# LUCID Module Reference

In-depth reference for every ES module in the LUCID codebase. Use this to
locate which module owns a given concern before editing. 

The codebase is split across four directories plus two root files:

- `pose/` — data model, triangulation/reprojection math, cross-view tracker.
- `ui/` — DOM-side controllers, overlays, panes, modals, viewport.
- `loading/` — video decoders, session-loader workflows, h5wasm workers.
- `import-export/` — file pickers, parsers, project save/load, SLP import.
- root — `app.js` entry point, `demo-data.js` synthetic dataset.

External script-tag globals (`three`, `mp4box`, `h5wasm`) and CDN ESM imports
(`dockview-core` — **pinned to 6.6.1**, see CLAUDE.md Dependencies) are not
listed under "Imports from project modules". Neither are importmap bare specifiers
(`mediabunny`, `h5wasm`, `pako`, `yaml`); `mediabunny` is vendored in
`lib/mediabunny/` and is used for video DECODE (via sleap-io.js) and video
ENCODE (via `ui/video-encode.js`). `yaml` maps to jsdelivr's single-file
`yaml@2.8.0/+esm` build (offline: the vendored `lib/yaml/browser/index.js`).
The script-tag globals load with `defer`: they no longer block the parser, but
they still run in order and before every module script, so a module may read
`THREE`, `MP4Box` or `h5wasm` at evaluation time exactly as before.

**Static imports have a deploy-time consequence.** A deployed copy's
`index.html` carries one `<link rel="modulepreload">` per module in the startup
graph — every module reachable from `app.js` and the sleap-io bridge through
static `import`/`export … from` — generated by `scripts/preload-modules.mjs`
(run by `scripts/stamp-version.mjs`; see CLAUDE.md Deployment). Nothing to maintain: adding a static import adds
its target to the list on the next deploy, and a dynamic `import()` stays lazy.

---

## pose/

### pose/initialization.js

**Purpose.** App startup logic. Builds the empty-session UI, wires the
`InteractionManager`, sets up the 3D viewport and timeline, and exposes
helpers used by every load path. Calls `init()` at module-load.

**Key exports.**
- `hideWelcomeOverlay()` — hides the dock empty-state overlay.
- `loadDemoSession()` — File menu "Load Demo Session" handler. Loads
  `sample_session/*.mp4` and synthetic data from `demo-data.js`.
- `addNewInstanceSmart()` — adds a new user instance to the focused view.
  Pose source priority: cached `lastUserPoints` → user instance on the current
  frame → user instance on the nearest **prior** frame (so ctrl+i inherits a
  labeled pose without first nudging a node) → nearest predicted instance →
  default BFS spread layout at the cursor.
- `setupInteraction()` — instantiates `InteractionManager` with all callback
  wiring (selection, drag, double-click, edit-group, etc.). Its
  `onInstanceDeleted` (Delete key, Edit ▸ Delete Instance, "- Instance") calls
  `markDirty()`, so the delete survives a session switch (which evicts the
  session's lazy store). Pinned by `tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs`
  cycle 5c.
- `setup3DViewport()` — instantiates `Viewport3D` and wires the
  "Show Camera View"/"Show Initial View" buttons. **Respects the `\` toggle**
  (`isViewport3DVisible()`, `ui/panel-visibility.js`): every session load
  funnels through here, so it never clears the `collapsed` class; when collapsed
  it disposes the old viewport, sets the singleton to `null` and builds no WebGL
  context — keeping it would show the PREVIOUS session's scene on expand.
- `update3DViewport(frameIdx)` — pushes current InstanceGroups into the 3D
  scene; auto-initializes the viewport if calibration is present. Returns
  immediately (no auto-init) when the panel is collapsed; the rebuild is a
  stateless function of the current frame, and `toggle3DViewport` calls back in
  here on expand.
- `navigateToFrame(frameIdx)` — unified frame navigation used by every UI entry
  point (timeline scrub/drag, transport buttons, arrow/Home/End keys). With a
  video controller it defers to `videoController.seekToFrame`; for a video-less
  project (skeleton + imported 3D points) it clamps to `[0, totalFrames-1]`,
  updates `state.currentFrame`, and re-renders overlays + seekbar + 3D viewport
  directly so the full points3d duration is navigable without a decoder.
- `setupTimeline()` — instantiates `Timeline` and wires its frame-change /
  range-select callbacks plus the display-mode button group. The frame-change /
  drag-end callbacks fall back to `navigateToFrame` when there's no video.
  Passes `getColorByIdentity` (reads `state.colorByIdentity`) so the
  timeline's track rows use the palette the canvas uses.
- `updateFpsDisplay()` — refreshes the FPS readout, and the frame readout's
  times (`refreshReadoutTotals`), which are frame / fps.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../ui/app-state.js` — `state`, controller singletons + setters, `VIEW_NAMES`,
  `isViewDocked` (the video controller's `isViewShown`).
- `./pose-data.js` — `Instance`, `UnlinkedInstance`, `points3dNodeCount`,
  `getPoint3d`, `groupDisplayName` (the group name in every interaction status
  line; never index `session.tracks` by a group's `trackIdx` (it has none) or
  its `identityId`).
- `./triangulation.js` — `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`, `updateTimelineForFrame`,
  `reTriangulateGroup`, `sessionHasCalibration`,
  `getOrComputeReprojectedInstance`.
- `../loading/video.js` — `OnDemandVideoDecoder`, `VideoController`.
- `../loading/session-loader.js` — `rebuildVideoController`.
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `markDirty`, `setStatus`, `showLoading`,
  `hideLoading`.
- `../import-export/plane-metadata.js` — `resetPlaneState` (demo load).
- `../demo-data.js` — `createDemoSession`.
- `../ui/ui-wiring.js` — `setupUI`, `setupMenus`, `updateSeekbar`,
  `onPlaybackStateChange`, `fitTimelineToData`.
- `../ui/frame-readout.js` — `refreshReadoutTotals`, from `updateFpsDisplay`.
- `../ui/timeline-controller.js` — `installTimelineShortcuts`.
- `../ui/info-tip.js` — `installInfoTips`, called FIRST in `init()`, before any
  panel renders: the listeners are delegated, so they must be in place before
  the first `[data-infotip]` element exists.
- `../ui/node-tooltip.js` — `installNodeTooltip`, right after it (the listeners
  that put the node tooltip away on a press, key, wheel, scroll or resize).
- `../ui/info-panel.js` — `setupPanelTabs`, `setupSkeletonEditing`,
  `setupVideosTab` (the Videos tab's Load/Remove buttons, wired once here
  because `updateInfoPanel` returns early with no session),
  `updateInfoPanel`.
- `../ui/plane-definition.js` — `setupPlaneDefinition` (called from `init()`
  right after `setupSkeletonEditing`, and **before** `paneManager.init` — its
  drop listeners are delegated on the `#videoDock` container, which is static
  markup, so panes added later are covered without re-wiring) and
  `planeInteractionCallbacks` (`Object.assign`ed onto the InteractionManager's
  callback bag in `setupInteraction`, so `ui/interaction.js` needs no import of
  the plane feature), plus `syncPlanes3D` / `refreshPlanePanel`.
- `../ui/layout-controls.js` — `setupSplitHandles`.
- `../ui/rendering.js` — `drawAllOverlays`, `setReprojErrorVisible`.
- `../ui/sessions-panes.js` — `populateViewStrip`, `populateSessionStrip`.
- `../ui/identity-assignment.js` — `manualAssignState`, `getTotalUnlinkedCount`,
  `cleanupManualAssignment`, `startManualAssignment`, `editGroupState`,
  `cancelEditGroup`, `finishEditGroup`, `updateEditGroupToast`,
  `purgeTriangulationDataForGroup`.
- `../ui/overlays.js` — `getTrackColor`, `getGroupColor`.
- `../ui/viewport3d.js` — `Viewport3D`.
- `../ui/panel-visibility.js` — `isViewport3DVisible`, `markViewport3DSkipped`.
- `../ui/timeline.js` — `Timeline`.
- `../ui/interaction.js` — `InteractionManager`.

**Imported by.** `app.js`, `pose/triangulation.js`,
`ui/export-modals.js`, `ui/origin-rebase.js`, `ui/sessions-panes.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`,
`loading/session-loader.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`.

**User-facing features.** App boot, demo session loader, smart Add-Instance
(`Mod+I`), 3D viewport auto-init, FPS display, all interaction
callbacks (selection status bar, drag/move feedback, double-click clone,
edit-group remove/add).

### pose/plane-fit.js

**Purpose.** The **one least-squares plane fit in the app**, and the symmetric
eigensolver it needs, in a module with no dependency but `pose/pose-data.js`.

**Why it exists as its own file.** `pose/plane-data.js` must fit planes (for
`planeLockForNode` / `constrainPoint3dForNode` / `usablePlaneFit`) but cannot
import `pose/triangulation.js`, which reaches into the UI and save-load — that
would break every Node test importing `plane-data.js` without loader hooks.
Duplicating the maths is worse: a derived fit must agree EXACTLY with the one
`Fit` stores, or a held corner moves AGAIN on the next Fit. One definition, two
importers.

**Key exports.**
- **`fitPlaneToPoints3d(points3d)`** — total-least-squares plane through the
  centroid (smallest-eigenvalue eigenvector of the covariance). Returns
  `{centroid, normal, rms, nPoints}`, or **null** for fewer than 3 present
  points, coincident points, or COLLINEAR ones (a pencil of planes; fitting an
  arbitrary member would silently rotate the annotation). **The normal's SIGN is
  arbitrary**, which is why `pose/plane-angle.js` measures an unsigned angle and
  the mesh exporters derive winding instead of trusting it.
- **`jacobiEigen(M, maxIter?, tol?)`** — Jacobi rotations for an NxN symmetric
  matrix, returning `{eigenvalues, eigenvectors}` (vectors as ROWS, paired by
  index, **unsorted**). `pose/triangulation-core.js` imports and re-exports it,
  so there is ONE implementation.
- **`planeIntersectionBasis(fits)`** → `{dirs, offsets, used}` with
  `dirs[j]·x = offsets[j]`: an orthonormal description of what several planes
  have in common (Gram-Schmidt over `[n | d]`), whose RANK is 1 a plane, 2 a
  line, 3 a point, 0 nothing — the geometry behind
  `PlaneModel.planeLockForNode`. A plane that adds no independent direction is
  **dropped, not averaged in**, below about 0.06° (`PLANE_INDEPENDENCE_TOL`,
  module-private): nearly-parallel planes meet in a line that runs off towards
  infinity, a worse answer than admitting the second plane says nothing new.
- **`projectOntoPlaneIntersection(xyz, basis)`** — the nearest point to `xyz`
  satisfying every constraint in `basis`, or null if the arithmetic did not stay
  finite. Exact, so re-projecting a point already on the subspace is a no-op; a
  rank-3 basis ignores `xyz` entirely.

**Imports from project modules.** `./pose-data.js` — `points3dNodeCount`,
`readPoint3d`. That module imports nothing at all, so this one is importable in
Node with no stubs — which is the point.

**Imported by.** `pose/triangulation.js` (which re-exports
`fitPlaneToPoints3d` unchanged, because every existing caller and test reads it
from there), `pose/triangulation-core.js` (`jacobiEigen`, re-exported) and
`pose/plane-data.js`. Note the core is worker-loaded, so nothing heavier than
`./pose-data.js` may ever be added to this module's imports.

**Tests.** Covered through its callers: `tests/test-plane-constrained-fit.mjs`,
`tests/test-plane-nodes.mjs` §20b (the derived fit) and §20c (the
intersection), `tests/test-plane-angle.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs` (the collinear and two-point refusals) and
`tests/e2e/plane-lock-holds.mjs`.

### pose/plane-nodes.js

**Purpose.** The **global pool of plane nodes**; a plane is an ordered list of
references into it. A shared pool is what lets two planes MEET along a shared
line: duplicated per-plane corners would be two independent 3D points that
drift apart the moment either plane is re-solved.

**Key exports.**
- `class PlaneNode` — `id` (stable, NEVER reused), `name`, `color`, `pin`,
  `xyz` (`Float64Array(3)`, all-NaN = untriangulated, matching
  `InstanceGroup.points3d`) and `error` (mean reprojection error, px).
  `hasPoint3d()` / `getPoint3d()` / `setPoint3d(xyz, {force})` /
  `clearPoint3d({force})`. **The node's `xyz` is the single source of truth for
  a plane node's 3D** — nothing else stores one, and every writer funnels
  through `setPoint3d`, which REFUSES on a `locked` node unless forced. That
  refusal is the entire locking mechanism.
- **TWO pin states**, because "do not move this" and "do not move this OFF ITS
  PLANE" are different promises:

  | `pin` | meaning |
  |---|---|
  | `'none'` | unpinned |
  | `'plane-locked'` | may move, but only within EVERY plane it is a member of |
  | `'locked'` | the 3D is frozen outright — what the constrained-fit subsystem calls an ANCHOR |

  `plane-locked` is **not** an anchor and must never reach
  `fitPlaneConstrained`: its position is not fixed. The two chokepoints every
  fit/triangulation consumer reads — `planeImmutableMask`
  (`ui/plane-definition.js`) and `planeNodeImmutability` (`plane-data.js`) —
  therefore report `locked` ONLY. Nor can `plane-locked` be enforced here: a
  node cannot resolve a plane's fit or find the planes referencing it, so
  `PlaneModel.planeLockForNode` / `constrainPoint3dForNode` own it. **The node
  carries no plane id**: what holds it is its MEMBERSHIP (one plane a surface,
  two a line, three a point), so adding or deleting a plane changes the
  constraint with no cascade. The planes need not have stored `planeFit`s — see
  `usablePlaneFit`.
- `immutable` is an **accessor** for `pin === 'locked'` (getter and setter), so
  `setPoint3d`'s refusal, `clearPoint3d`, `mutableIds`, `nodeFreezeState` and the
  ~30 readers written against the boolean work unchanged. The setter collapses
  `plane-locked` → `'none'` on `false` ("unpin this node").
- `nodePinState(node)` → the tri-state value; the accessor new code should read,
  since `immutable` cannot tell `plane-locked` from `none`. `normalizePin(v)`
  coerces anything (including the legacy boolean and a malformed saved value)
  into a valid state, defaulting to `'none'` rather than throwing — a malformed
  project file must load. `PIN_STATES` lists them in increasing strictness.
- `class PlaneNodePool` — the ordered pool. Its order is the canonical index
  space every `PlaneInstance` is keyed by, so it owns the operations that
  change it: `addNode(name, {color, immutable, pin})`
  (`pin` wins over `immutable` when both are given), `removeNode(id)`,
  `moveNode(from, to)` (the last two are LOW LEVEL — go through `PlaneModel`,
  which keeps the per-view 2D in step). Plus `getNode(id)`, `nodeAt(i)`,
  `indexOf(id)`, `has(id)`, `ids()`/`names()`/`colors()`, `setImmutable`,
  `setPin(id, pin)` (the only way to reach `plane-locked`, since `setImmutable`
  is a boolean and can only express the two ends; it takes NO plane, because
  membership is the constraint and there is nothing here to nominate),
  `freezeState`, `hasPoint3d`/`getPoint3d`/`setPoint3d`/`clearPoint3d` by id,
  `points3d()` (flat, pool order, freshly allocated) and `mutableIds()`.
  `adoptNode(node)` is the RESTORE-path twin of `addNode`: it KEEPS the node's
  ID, because plane membership, plane edges and every placement's `nodeIds`
  ledger are stored as IDs, and advances `_nextId` past it so IDs are still
  never reused. Used by `pose/plane-serialization.js`.
- `nodeFreezeState(node)` → `'mutable' | 'frozen' | 'frozen-unsolved'`, keyed on
  `locked` alone (a `plane-locked` node reports `'mutable'`). **Keep the three
  values**: the return is used verbatim as a CSS class (`'plane-node-' + st`)
  and keyed by `nodeStateTitle` and `styles.css`. The pin is settable before
  anything is triangulated, but a pinned node with no 3D can never ACQUIRE one,
  so that dead end is NAMED (`frozen-unsolved`) rather than hidden.
- `PLANE_NODE_COLORS`, `defaultNodeColor(i)`.

**Why per-node `Float64Array(3)` and not one flat pool array.** A flat array
would have to be spliced in step with every add/remove/reorder — the
index-shifting fragility this module exists to remove. Callers wanting the flat
form ask for a fresh one (`points3d()` here, `points3dForPlane()` in
`plane-data.js`).

**Imports from project modules.** None (deliberately dependency-free).

**Imported by.** `pose/plane-data.js` (which re-exports `PlaneNode`,
`PlaneNodePool`, `PLANE_NODE_COLORS`, `defaultNodeColor` and `nodeFreezeState`),
`pose/plane-serialization.js`, `ui/plane-definition.js` and
`ui/plane-nodes-panel.js` (`PIN_STATES`).

**Tests.** `tests/test-plane-nodes.mjs`,
`tests/test-plane-serialization.mjs` (the `adoptNode` restore path).

---

### pose/plane-data.js

**Purpose.** Data model for user-annotated **planes** (which feed the origin
frame, mesh objects and plane angles). Owns the types annotation produces and
nothing about the solve or the UI, which live in `pose/triangulation.js` and
`ui/plane-definition.js`.

**Key exports.**
- `class PlaneSkeleton` — one plane: `nodeIds[]` (an ORDERED LIST OF NODE
  REFERENCES into the pool), `edges` as pairs of **NODE IDS**, `name`, `color`,
  `filled`, `triangulation` (`{views, nNodes, meanError}`) and `planeFit`
  (`{centroid, normal, rms, nPoints}`). Membership and
  edges are IDs, never index pairs: an index pair breaks the instant the pool
  shifts or a node is shared. `size`, `hasNode`/`indexOfNode`/`addNode(id)`/
  `removeNode(id)` (which drops the edges through it)/`moveNode`,
  `addEdge`/`hasEdge`/`removeEdge`/`removeEdgeBetween`, `clearTriangulation()`.
  **Deliberately NOT a `Skeleton` subclass** — `Skeleton` models nodes it OWNS
  (`addNode(name)` mints one, `removeNode(idx)` renumbers edges by index), both
  wrong for references into a shared pool.
  **`clearTriangulation()` drops `planeFit` + `triangulation` and NOTHING
  ELSE** — it cannot reach a node's 3D. It is called on every 2D edit,
  placement change and node-list change, so if it could null 3D, a pinned
  node's surveyed coordinate would evaporate on an unrelated nudge.
- `class PlaneInstance extends Instance` — **one per VIEW**, covering the whole
  pool (index = pool index), not one per (view, plane), so `ui/interaction.js`
  drags a plane node through the SAME accessor path as a UserInstance. Carries `id`, `viewName`,
  `type: 'plane'`, `nulledNodes` (Set of POOL INDICES; same field name and
  meaning as a UserInstance's, so the inherited `hasAnyUsablePoint()` already
  reads it), **`derivedNodes`** (Set of POOL INDICES whose 2D here was
  REPROJECTED from the 3D rather than annotated — see below), `placedPlanes`
  (Set of PLANE IDS — explicit, because with shared nodes "this node has a
  position here" can no longer tell you which planes the user placed) and
  **`nodeIds`** (the NODE ID of each column). Frame-independent by design.
  - `nodeIds` is a LEDGER, not a second addressing scheme: callers still address
    the instance by POOL INDEX. It exists because **a count is not an
    identity**: a DETACHED instance (per-session placement maps) can miss a
    mid-pool splice or move, and re-syncing by node COUNT re-seats every column
    past the edit onto its neighbour's node with every point still looking
    valid. Matching by ID is the only repair that can tell those cases apart.
  - `isNodeNulled(i)` / `toggleNodeNull(i)` / `isPlanePlaced(planeId)`,
    `centroid(indices?)` (restrict to one plane's nodes for its label).
  - `isNodeDerived(i)` / `setNodeDerived(i, on)` / `clearDerivedNodes(indices?)`
    — the REPROJECTED flag. A point `triangulatePlane` reprojected into a view
    is not evidence: feeding it back would drag the reported error toward zero
    every time the button is pressed. So it renders and drags like any other,
    but is excluded from the solve and the error average until the user DRAGS
    it. Deliberately separate from `nulledNodes`: a right-click-off must not
    silently promote a reprojection to an annotation.
  - `_indexFlagSets()` — every index-keyed flag set (`nulledNodes`,
    `derivedNodes`) in one list, which the three index-remapping paths
    (`resyncToNodeIds` / `removeNodeAt` / `moveNodeAt`) all iterate. A set left
    un-remapped silently points at its NEIGHBOUR's node, so add any new
    per-(view, node) set here.
  - `appendNode(id)` — one UNPOSITIONED column: a node only becomes visible on a
    view once a plane referencing it is placed there, which is where seeding
    happens.
  - `resyncToNodeIds(ids)` — re-seat the columns onto `ids` BY NODE ID; a
    survivor keeps its point and per-node flags, a dropped node's column goes, a
    new node arrives unpositioned. The repair path for a detached map, used by
    `attachPlacements` / `ensureInstance`.
  - `seedNodeNear(idx, cx, cy, ordinal, videoW, videoH)` — golden-angle spoke,
    for a node joining an ALREADY-placed plane (an unpositioned node draws
    nothing, so there would be nothing to grab).
  - `removeNodeAt(i)` / `moveNodeAt(from, to)` — mirror the pool's splice/move,
    carrying the per-node flag sets with their nodes.
- `class PlaneModel` — pool + planes + `placements` (`Map<viewName,
  PlaneInstance>`) + `meshObjects` (a `MeshObjectSet`, see
  `pose/mesh-object-3d.js` — purely ADDITIVE: no method here reads or maintains
  it, and `deletePlane` deliberately does NOT cascade into it, because membership
  resolves lazily), and every operation that spans them. Doing any of these
  piecemeal corrupts the index space: `createPlane`/`getPlane`/`deletePlane`
  (**deletes NO nodes — every one survives**),
  `adoptPlane(plane)` (the restore-path twin of `createPlane`, keeping the
  plane's ID because `PlaneInstance.placedPlanes` stores plane IDs — see
  `PlaneNodePool.adoptNode`),
  `planesForNode(id)`, `planeLockForNode(nodeId)`,
  `constrainPoint3dForNode(nodeId, xyz)`, `usableFitForPlane(planeOrId)`,
  `ensureInstance`/`getInstance`/`views`/`allInstances`,
  `attachPlacements(map)` (adopt a session-owned map, re-syncing every instance
  to the pool and pruning placement flags for deleted planes),
  `addNode`/`deleteNode`/`moveNode` (pool + every view's 2D + every plane's
  membership and edges), `addNodeToPlane`/`createNodeInPlane`/
  `removeNodeFromPlane`, `placePlane`/`unplacePlane`/`isPlanePlaced`/
  `placedViews`/`placedPlanes`, `isNodeVisibleOn`/`visibleNodeIndices`/
  `planeNodeIndices`, `invalidateNode3D(id)` and `invalidatePlane3D(plane)`.
- **Deleting a plane deletes no nodes, ever.** A node no plane references is
  still a valid pool member, removed only by an explicit `deleteNode`.
  Auto-deleting "orphans" would destroy exactly the nodes worth keeping — a
  pinned surveyed reference, a corner staged for a plane not built yet — and
  deletion is the one path no re-fit can undo. `removeNodeFromPlane` follows the
  same rule (`deleteIfOrphan` defaults to FALSE).
- **`planeLockForNode(nodeId)`** → `{rank, planes, basis}`: what a
  `plane-locked` node may move in. `rank` — **0** free, **1** a plane's surface,
  **2** the line two planes meet in, **3** the single point three meet at;
  `planes` is every plane holding it that has a usable fit. The planes are its
  MEMBERSHIP (`planesForNode`), never a nominated id, so there is no second
  record of "which plane" to disagree with the model. Each plane contributes a
  USABLE fit with the node EXCLUDED (`usablePlaneFit`); the geometry, including
  which planes are independent enough to count, is `planeIntersectionBasis` in
  `pose/plane-fit.js`.
- **`constrainPoint3dForNode(nodeId, xyz)`** is the SINGLE place the
  `plane-locked` pin state is honoured: returns the NEAREST position satisfying
  every plane the node belongs to (`projectOntoPlaneIntersection`), so an edit
  that already satisfies them is taken verbatim and re-applying it is a no-op.
  A node that is not `plane-locked`, or that nothing can currently hold, passes
  through untouched. It cannot live on `PlaneNode` (where `locked` is enforced)
  because a node can neither resolve a plane's fit nor find the planes
  referencing it.
- **`usablePlaneFit(plane, pool, excludeNodeId?)`** / **
  `PlaneModel.usableFitForPlane(planeOrId)`** — the stored `planeFit`, else one
  derived with `fitPlaneToPoints3d` from the plane's own solved corners, writing
  nothing. **Requiring a STORED fit would make `plane-locked` inert**: `setNodePin`
  (`ui/plane-nodes-panel.js`) clears the `planeFit` of every plane holding the
  node whose pin changed — including the planes holding it.
  **`excludeNodeId` is not an optimisation**: including the node being
  constrained lets a derived plane CHASE it, so every repeat edit drifts
  further; excluding it makes the plane the OTHER corners define and makes
  re-projecting a committed position a no-op. A STORED fit is used unexcluded
  (it does not move when a corner is nudged). Fewer than 3 other solved corners,
  or collinear ones, derive nothing and the constraint stays inert.
  `ui/plane-angle.js`'s `usableFit` delegates here, so the dialog and the pin
  cannot disagree about where a plane is.
- **`force` never rides a cascade.** `setPoint3d`/`clearPoint3d`'s `force` means
  one narrow thing — explicit user re-entry of a pinned coordinate. Invalidation
  fires off edits made somewhere else entirely, so it does NOT forward its
  options to the leaf: `invalidateNode3D` / `invalidatePlane3D` take the
  separately named `forceClearImmutable`, and a stray `{force:true}` in their
  options cannot strip a pinned node.
- Per-plane materializers, all freshly allocated (the pool stays the single
  source of truth): `points3dForPlane(plane, pool)` → flat `Float64Array(3n)`
  in PLANE order, all-NaN triple for a node with no 3D — the shape the 3D
  viewport payload, the fit and the origin frame already read;
  `writePoints3dForPlane(plane, pool, flat, {force, constrain})` →
  `{written, skippedIds, constrainedIds}` (the ONLY way a solve should publish;
  it SKIPS `locked` nodes and says which). The optional
  `constrain(nodeId, xyz) → xyz` hook is threaded HERE because this is the one
  sanctioned publish path, so every solve (triangulate, fit, the angle edit)
  honours a `plane-locked` node without having to remember to. Callers holding
  a model pass `constrainPoint3dForNode`; the pure tests pass nothing;
  `nodeErrorsForPlane`, `planeNodes`/`planeNodeNames`/`planeNodeColors`/
  `planeNodeImmutability` (a corner shared between two planes reads pinned in
  BOTH, which lets a frozen shared node hold an intersection line still while
  either plane is re-fitted. It reports `locked` ONLY — a `plane-locked` node is
  not an anchor for `fitPlaneConstrained`) /
  `planeNodeIndices`, `planeEdgesLocal` (plane-order index pairs, for the 3D
  payload) / `planeEdgesPoolIndices` (for the 2D overlay + interaction hit
  test), `planeCentroid2d(plane, pool, instance)`.
- `planeCycleOrderIds(plane)` — the RING the user drew, as node IDs, or `null`
  when their connections do not form one. A ring is a single simple cycle
  covering every node the plane references (every node at degree exactly 2, one
  closed walk); an open chain, a partial or disjoint cycle, a branch, or no
  edges at all returns null — guessing the rest is how a fill self-intersects.
  Purely topological, so it is the ONLY form that can express a **concave**
  outline (an L-shaped floor), and nothing may second-guess it.
- `planePolygonOrderIds(plane)` — the ring when there is one, else MEMBERSHIP
  order, as node IDs; `planePolygonOrder(plane)` (plane-local indices) and
  `planePolygonOrderPoolIndices(plane, pool)` are its two index views. **The
  fill does not use these** (see below).
- `convexHullOrder2d(pts)` / `convexHullOrder3d(qs, normal?)` — hull of a point
  set as indices into it, in ring order (interior, on-edge and duplicate points
  dropped). Fewer than 3 hull vertices returns the input order unchanged, so a
  caller's own `< 3` guard still works. The 3D form projects onto an in-plane
  basis (`normal` if given, else estimated), which only ever decides vertex
  ORDER, never a coordinate.
- `planeFillOrderPoolIndices(plane, pool, instance)` (2D, pool indices) /
  `planeFillOrder3d(plane, pool)` (3D, plane-local indices) — **what the fill
  actually walks.** The user's ring when they drew one, otherwise the CONVEX
  HULL of the plane's points. Membership order would fill a quad added out of
  ring order as a bowtie, and would carve a notch to a node placed in the
  MIDDLE of a plane (a reflex vertex); under the hull an interior node sits
  INSIDE the fill. Hulled per VIEW in 2D, because the 2D fill exists before
  anything is triangulated. Unpositioned nodes contribute no vertex; a node with
  2D but no 3D is a vertex of the 2D fill and not the 3D one.
- `seedPlanePoints(n, cx, cy, videoW, videoH)` — node 0 at 12 o'clock, the rest
  evenly around a ring of `PLACEMENT_RADIUS_FRAC` (12%) of the video's shorter
  side, clamped into the frame.
- `PLANE_COLORS`, `PLACEMENT_RADIUS_FRAC`, and everything re-exported from
  `pose/plane-nodes.js`.

**Nodes are plane-independent.** A plane REFERENCES nodes; it does not own
them. The same node may belong to any number of planes, which is what lets two
planes meet along a shared line — the corners on it are ONE node with ONE 3D
position, so the line cannot split when either plane is re-triangulated.
`planeFit` stays per-plane because two planes through the same edge genuinely
have different fits; the triangulated POINTS are what is shared.

**Placement membership is explicit; un-placing keeps 2D.** Visibility is
derived from `placedPlanes` (`isNodeVisibleOn`), never from the points.
Un-placing a plane from a view does NOT destroy 2D — re-placing restores it,
and `placePlane` seeds only nodes with no position yet. Deleting a NODE is what
destroys 2D, everywhere at once.

**Isolation.** PlaneInstances live on `PlaneModel.placements`, NOT in
`frameGroups` / `instanceGroups`. Nothing in the existing pose pipeline can
see them, so `type: 'plane'` never has to be handled by code that switches on
user/predicted/reprojected.

**Imports from project modules.** `./pose-data.js` — `Instance`;
`./plane-nodes.js` — the pool (re-exported); `./mesh-object-3d.js`;
`./plane-fit.js` — `fitPlaneToPoints3d` for `usablePlaneFit`, plus
`planeIntersectionBasis` / `projectOntoPlaneIntersection` for
`planeLockForNode`. DOM-free and off `pose/triangulation.js` (see
`pose/plane-fit.js`).

**Imported by.** `ui/plane-definition.js`, `ui/plane-angle.js`,
`ui/plane-list-panel.js`, `ui/plane-nodes-panel.js`, `ui/plane-overlays.js`,
`pose/plane-serialization.js`, `pose/plane-angle.js`,
`pose/mesh-object-geometry.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-plane-nodes.mjs` (§20/§20b/§20c for the pin constraint,
the derived fit with the exclusion's negative control, and the
membership-intersection ladder),
`tests/test-plane-serialization.mjs`, `tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/plane-lock-holds.mjs`.

---

### pose/plane-serialization.js

**Purpose.** The Define Planes feature's **on-disk form, both ways** — the node
pool, the planes (membership, edges, fill, the solve summary, the plane fit),
the per-view 2D and the origin frame, to plain JSON and back. Owns the mapping
and NOTHING about where the bytes end up; `import-export/plane-metadata.js`
decides that. DOM-free so the round trip is directly testable — a subtly wrong
restore does not crash, it seats corners on their NEIGHBOURS' nodes.

**The scope split it encodes.** PROJECT-scoped: the pool, the planes, the origin
frame — one node has one 3D position for the whole project, which is the entire
reason the pool is global. SESSION-scoped: the `PlaneInstance` per view, because
a view belongs to a session (stored on `Session.planePlacements`).

**Key exports.**
- `serializePlaneNodes(pool)` / `restorePlaneNodes(data)` — pool order is
  preserved because it IS the index space every `PlaneInstance` is keyed by. A
  node with no 3D **omits `xyz` entirely** rather than writing three numbers:
  `JSON.stringify(NaN)` is `null`, so a naive round trip returns
  `[null, null, null]` and every `isFinite` check downstream has to defend
  against it. A locked node's coordinate is restored with `{force: true}` —
  being locked is exactly what refuses an ordinary write. Entries with a missing
  or DUPLICATE id are skipped, never renumbered.
- **The two pin states, written so BOTH directions degrade correctly.** A
  `locked` node writes `pin: 'locked'` **and**, redundantly, `immutable: true`,
  the only key an older build reads, so it still freezes. A `plane-locked` node
  writes `pin` alone and deliberately **no** `immutable`: an older build cannot
  enforce "stays in its planes", and treating it as free is the honest fallback.
  **No plane id rides along** — membership is already under `planes`; a legacy
  `pinPlaneId` is read and DISCARDED. On restore `pin` wins when present, and a
  legacy `immutable: true` means `locked`; both go through `normalizePin`, so an
  unrecognized value loads as unpinned. An unpinned node writes NEITHER key
  (defaults are never written — `tests/e2e/save-golden-digest.mjs` does not
  move), and this rides the existing `planeNodes` key, so
  `PLANE_METADATA_KEYS` does not change.
- `serializePlanes(planes)` / `restorePlanes(data, pool)` — includes `filled`,
  `triangulation` and `planeFit`, because those are the plane's STATE, not a
  cache (an un-fit reopened plane offers Set Origin nothing). References to
  nodes the pool does not hold are **dropped** (one corner short, which is
  visible) rather than kept dangling (a -1 index reads a plausible but wrong
  point, which is not).
- `serializeMeshObjects(set)` / `restoreMeshObjects(data, planeIds)` — the named
  groups of planes. Membership is written VERBATIM, **including IDs whose plane
  has since been deleted** (membership resolves lazily everywhere else); the
  READ side drops unresolvable IDs, which is why `restorePlaneProject` restores
  objects LAST, after the planes. An object writes exactly
  `id`/`name`/`color`/`planeIds` — NO orientation key, because winding is
  derived; a legacy `flipNormals` is read and DISCARDED, since re-applying it
  would invert objects the derivation already gets right.
- `serializePlanePlacements(placements, pool)` / `restorePlanePlacements(data,
  pool, planeIds)` — points are written as `{n: <nodeId>, xy, off, derived}`,
  **not** as a dense pool-order array, which could only be re-seated by
  COUNTING (see the `PlaneInstance.nodeIds` ledger in `pose/plane-data.js`). A
  view holding no positioned point and no placed plane contributes nothing.
- `serializeOriginFrame(frame)` / `restoreOriginFrame(data)` — writes only the
  **two things the user chose** (the origin and the +Z, plus the
  `sourcePlane`/`sourceNode` labels, which cannot be re-derived once a plane is
  renamed). `R`, the translation and the axis-angle form are rebuilt exactly by
  `buildOriginFrame`, so storing them would be a second source of truth.
- `serializePlaneProject(model)` / `restorePlaneProject(model, data)` — the
  project-scoped bundle: pool + planes + mesh objects. `restore` **REPLACES** all
  three rather than merging: two projects' node IDs are unrelated, and
  renumbering is the one thing this module must never do. An empty-but-named
  plane or object still round-trips; only a model with no nodes, no planes AND
  no objects serializes to `null`.

**Absence is a value.** Every serializer returns `null` when there is nothing to
say, so a project that never opened the feature writes no keys and its bytes are
unchanged (`tests/e2e/save-golden-digest.mjs`). Every reader tolerates absent,
malformed and partial input — a `.slp` from SLEAP, or from a LUCID build older
than this module, restores an empty model rather than throwing.

**Imports from project modules.** `./plane-nodes.js` — `PlaneNode`,
`PlaneNodePool`; `./plane-data.js` — `PlaneSkeleton`, `PlaneInstance`;
`./mesh-object-3d.js` — `MeshObject3D`; `./origin-frame.js` — `buildOriginFrame`.

**Imported by.** `import-export/plane-metadata.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-plane-serialization.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/plane-persistence-roundtrip.mjs` (through the real app and a real
`.slp`).

---

### pose/origin-frame.js

**Purpose.** Turns "this annotated node is the origin, and +Z points that way"
into the translation + rotation that re-express the calibration's world frame in
the user's frame. DOM-free and dependency-free.

**Convention, stated once and used everywhere:**

    p_new = R · p_old + t,    t = −R · origin

`R`'s **rows** are the new frame's axes in old-world coordinates. `t` is the
mapping's translation, **not** the origin's position — both are reported and
both are labelled in the UI, because confusing them silently flips a sign. The
inverse is `p_old = Rᵀ · p_new + origin`.

**Key exports.**
- `buildOriginFrame(origin, zAxis, xHint)` → `{origin, xAxis, yAxis, zAxis, R,
  translation, rotationVector, axis, angleRad, angleDeg}` or **null**. Z is the
  user's choice; X is old world +X projected onto the plane (old +Y when Z is
  within ~26° of X), `Y = Z × X` — an arbitrary but **consistent** roll, so the
  same two clicks always give the same rotation. `xHint` is the hook for a
  meaningful X (a plane edge, say). Returns null on non-finite input or a
  zero-length Z rather than inventing a frame.
- `rotationMatrixToAxisAngle(R)` → `{axis, angleRad}`. Handles angle ≈ 0
  (returns +X and 0) and angle ≈ π (from `R + I = 2nnᵀ`). **The π case is not
  exotic here** — it is what picking the "blue" arrow produces.
- `rotationAboutAxis(axis, angleRad)` → row-major 3×3 (closed-form Rodrigues),
  or **null** for a zero-length or non-finite axis. **The exact inverse of
  `rotationMatrixToAxisAngle`**, and tested against it. The same formula also
  appears inline in `Camera.rotationMatrix` (`pose/pose-data.js`), coupled to an
  OpenCV `rvec`; deliberately left alone.
- `mulMat3Vec3(R, v)` → `R · v`, no translation — what rotating a DIRECTION (a
  plane normal) needs, as against `applyOriginFrame`, which is an affine map of
  a POINT. Confusing the two is how a rotated normal picks up a translation.
- `applyOriginFrame(frame, p)` / `unapplyOriginFrame(frame, q)`.
- `rebaseExtrinsics(camR, camT, frame)` → `{R, rvec, tvec}` or **null** — the
  **calibration half of the origin change**: `R_new = R_cam·Rᵀ`,
  `t_new = R_cam·origin + t_cam`. **The translation is built from
  `frame.origin`, not `frame.translation`** — the two differ by a rotation, and
  substituting one produces a calibration that still almost works. Intrinsics
  and distortion are not its business. Returns both notations because the TOML
  writer matches the source file's shape.
- `normalize3` / `cross3` / `dot3`.

**Imports from project modules.** None, deliberately.

**Imported by.** `ui/origin-definition.js`, `pose/origin-rebase.js`
(`rebaseExtrinsics` + the point/direction transforms), `pose/plane-serialization.js`
(`buildOriginFrame`, to rebuild a saved frame from its two inputs),
`pose/mesh-object-geometry.js` (`applyOriginFrame` only),
`pose/plane-angle.js` (the rotation primitive plus the vector helpers) and
`pose/calibration-compare.js` (`rotationAboutAxis`,
`rotationMatrixToAxisAngle`, `normalize3`).

**Tests.** `tests/test-origin-frame.mjs` (§9 the `rotationAboutAxis` ↔
`rotationMatrixToAxisAngle` round trip; §10 `rebaseExtrinsics` — a point lands
on the same camera coordinates before and after, with the `frame.translation`
negative control) and `tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs` §14 end to end.

### pose/origin-rebase.js

**Purpose.** Move the **whole project** into the defined origin — the engine
behind the Danger Zone's `Set as New Calibration`. Unlike
`ui/origin-definition.js` (which changes only what is DRAWN), this rewrites
every stored 3D number so the project IS that frame.

**Three transforms, and they must all happen together:**

| thing | rule |
|---|---|
| 3D point | `p' = R·p + t` (affine) |
| 3D direction (a plane normal) | `n' = R·n` — **no translation** |
| camera | `R_cam' = R_cam·Rᵀ`, `t_cam' = R_cam·origin + t_cam` |

Do all three and **every 2D pixel is unchanged**: a point's reprojection under
the new calibration lands exactly where it landed under the old one. That
invariant is the whole safety argument, and it is what both test files assert
first. Move the points without the cameras (or vice versa) and every
reprojection error in the project silently explodes. So the set is not
negotiable: `InstanceGroup.points3d` across every session it is handed, the
plane node pool, each plane's stored fit, and every camera. 2D data is
invariant and is not touched.

**The `sessions` argument is the SUBSET the user chose** — no function here
takes a "which sessions" flag, so there is no partial code path to drift. A
session not in it keeps BOTH its 3D and its cameras, so it stays internally
consistent. The plane pool and plane fits are project-scoped and move exactly
once, with the active session, which the dialog (`ui/origin-rebase.js`) does
not allow to be deselected.

**Key exports.**
- `countRebaseTargets(sessions, model)` → the tally the confirmation dialog
  lists. **`tally.points3d` is every 3D point that moves — `keypoints3d` PLUS
  `planeNodes`** — and is what the headline quotes. A plane FIT stays out of the
  sum (a centroid and a normal, not a point).
  Groups are bucketed by their MEMBERS' types (`user` / `predicted` /
  neither), since `points3d` carries no provenance, and counted **both ways**:
  `userGroups` / `predictedGroups` / `untypedGroups` and
  `userKeypoints3d` / `predictedKeypoints3d` / `untypedKeypoints3d`.
  `reprojectedInstances` is reported as **unchanged**.
  **`userInstances` / `predictedInstances` are the WHOLE 2D population**
  (most of an imported `.slp` is ungrouped predictions in
  `FrameGroup.unlinkedInstances`). The enumeration mirrors
  `ui/custom-delete-ops.js`'s `collectSessionWide`:
  `lazyLoader.forEachInstanceRow` (duck-typed, no import) on a lazy project,
  because `session.frameGroups` there is a small resident window; group members
  plus the unlinked pool on an eager one, which are disjoint by construction.
  `instanceScope` (`'store'` / `'resident'`) says which answered, and
  `userMembers` / `predictedMembers` keep the grouped subtotal.
  **`perSession` is the same record once per session**, holding only what is
  session-scoped: cameras, pose 3D, the 2D population, `planePoints2d` (counted
  in POINTS). The plane pool and fits are project-scoped and appear only in the
  totals. Every per-session field sums to its total
  (`tests/test-origin-rebase.mjs` §1d).
- `subsetRebaseTally(full, keep)` → the same tally re-totalled over a SUBSET of
  its sessions, `keep(perSessionRecord, index)` choosing them, so the dialog's
  numbers follow its checkboxes without re-walking a lazy project's store per
  click. A re-FOLD of `full.perSession` sharing one private
  `foldPerSessionTotals` with `countRebaseTargets`, so it equals a fresh count by
  construction (§11). `planeNodes` / `planeFits` are carried over VERBATIM.
- `planOriginRebase(sessions, model, frame, opts)` → a plan, `null` when
  cancelled or the frame is unusable, or `{failed, camera}` when one camera
  cannot be re-based (which abandons the WHOLE plan — a project with one camera
  left in the old frame is worse than one that did not move). **Writes
  nothing:** it builds the replacement buffers beside the live ones, so
  cancelling is free and exact. Yields every `opts.yieldEvery` (default 2,000)
  units and asks `opts.shouldCancel()` there.
- `applyOriginRebase(plan)` → swaps them in, synchronously. Plane nodes are
  written straight to `node.xyz`, **bypassing `PlaneNode.setPoint3d`'s refusal
  for a Locked node**: a pin says "do not re-solve this point", not "exempt this
  point from the world moving".

**Why copy-on-write rather than transform-in-place-and-invert.** The second
would halve the peak, but `Rᵀ(R·p + t − t)` is not bit-identical to `p`, so a
*cancelled* operation would leave every coordinate perturbed.

**It is an O(groups) in-memory walk even on a lazily-reopened project.**
`session.instanceGroups` is built in full at load by BOTH reconstructors in
`import-export/slp-import.js`; only the 2D `frameGroups` are windowed. So there
is no hydration sweep and no resident-only hazard here.

**Imports from project modules.** `./origin-frame.js` (`applyOriginFrame`,
`mulMat3Vec3`, `rebaseExtrinsics`), `./pose-data.js` (`pooledPoints3d` — `applyOriginRebase` stores each re-based group in the slab pool — `points3dNodeCount`,
`hasPoint3d`, `isPoints3dFromFile` / `markPoints3dFromFile` — a group whose 3D
was read from a file keeps that mark across the re-base, so the display still
reprojects it rather than re-solving — and `unsmoothedPoints3d` /
`markPoints3dSmoothed`: a smoothed group's pre-smoothing copy is re-based by the
same `rebasePoints3d` (the plan's `nextUnsmoothed`), so a later smoothing run
filters 3D in the new frame). DOM-free.

**Imported by.** `ui/origin-rebase.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-origin-rebase.mjs` (the pixel invariant, planning writes
nothing, cancel is byte-identical, the Locked node moving anyway, the
centroid/normal split, §11 the SUBSET case) and
`tests/e2e/set-new-calibration.mjs`.

### pose/detection-filter-stats.js

**Purpose.** The tracker's three detection filters (`filterMinVisibleNodes`,
`filterMinInstanceScore`, `filterMinMeanNodeScore`) — the rule itself, which
`pose/tracker.js` imports and single-camera Track All
(`pose/single-camera-tracking.js`) applies before SLEAP's tracker — and the
count of what they would remove, per camera
and in total, before anyone runs tracking, so the Tracking Wizard can show it
while the values are being chosen (`ui/detection-filter-preview.js`). DOM-free,
no imports.

**Why it exists.** The filters drop detections silently, and they do not hit
every camera alike: on a real 3-camera rig the low side camera's detections
scored far below the other two (median mean node score 0.64), so a value that
is harmless for two cameras empties the third.

**Key exports.**
- `failsMinVisibleNodes(visible, min)`, `failsMinInstanceScore(score, min)`,
  `failsMinMeanNodeScore(mean, min)` — the three primitives. An instance score
  that is null/undefined/NaN never fails (the tracker's rule); a score of 0
  does. A NaN mean node score (no node scores) never fails.
- `meanNodeScore(inst, session?, camName?, frameIdx?)` — sleap-nn's "mean node
  score": the sum of the PRESENT nodes' scores over ALL the skeleton's nodes, a
  missing node counting as 0. Averaging the present nodes only understated the
  real stressTest sideC rerun (36.7% below 0.75 instead of sleap-nn's 43.8%).
  That is sleap-nn 0.3.3's definition; 0.2.0 averaged the present nodes only,
  so a file it filtered still shows a few detections below its own value here.
  NaN for a user instance (only a prediction was scored) and for a prediction
  with no node scores. Eager: `Instance.pointScore`. Lazy: straight from the
  store row the instance was hydrated from (`_rawInstIndex`), presence from the
  instance, no name lookup (a mean does not depend on node order) — cheap
  enough for every detection of a Track All.
- `passesDetectionFilter(inst, minVis, minScore, minMeanNode?, session?,
  camName?, frameIdx?)` — **the tracker's filter**, multi-camera and
  single-camera alike; every minimum 0 is a no-op, and the node score is
  computed only when its minimum is set. A loaded user instance's score is 0,
  so a minimum instance score above 0 removes it too (the readout counts it).
- `collectDetectionStats(session)` / `collectDetectionStatsAsync(session,
  {isCancelled})` — walk the session ONCE into flat typed columns. The async
  one walks each camera in windows of `ASYNC_WINDOW_FRAMES` (4,000) frames with
  a yield between them: one task per CAMERA held the page ~300 ms per camera on
  the real 5-mouse project (108,000 frames x 8 cameras, 4.2M detections, lazy),
  where the whole walk now takes ~2.7 s with no task over ~0.1 s. Columns: camera index, present-node count, instance
  score (Float64 — a stored 0.9 must still pass a minimum of 0.9), mean node
  score (the same arithmetic as `meanNodeScore`, store rows included),
  predicted flag; plus per-camera `detections` / `predicted` /
  `predictedWithScore` / `withNodeScores`, and `hasInstanceScores` /
  `hasNodeScores`.
- `countDetectionFilters(stats, {minVisibleNodes, minInstanceScore,
  minMeanNodeScore}, included?)` — per camera and over the `included` cameras:
  `failVisible`, `failScore`, `failNodeScore`, `removed` (any — what tracking
  drops) and `failsMultiple` (two or more). One pass over the columns; this is
  what a keystroke costs.
- `detectionHistograms(stats, bins)` — per camera, how each filtered quantity
  is spread: `visible` (one bin per node count, 0 … the largest in the
  session), `score` (predictions only) and `nodeScore` (detections with node
  scores), the last two in equal-width bins over [0, 1] (above 1 lands in the
  last), each with its median. One pass, no sort: the score medians come from
  1,000 bins per unit (exact to 0.001, finer than the two decimals shown) —
  sorting each camera's values held the page ~0.9 s on that same project.
- `flagOutlierCameras(rows, partKey, wholeKey?)` — the cameras that lose MUCH
  more than the rest, which the total row hides (real HardFight, sideC
  unfiltered, at the inference values 12 / 0.35 / 0.6: sideC 24.8%, the other
  cameras 0.7–2.2%, total 2.4%). Each tracked camera with detections is compared
  with the MEDIAN share of the OTHER tracked cameras (never with the total,
  which it is part of) and flagged when it is at least `OUTLIER_MIN_EXCESS`
  (5 points) above it AND at least `OUTLIER_MIN_RATIO` (2x) it — so 0.4%
  against 0.1% is not news, and when every camera loses a lot none is singled
  out. Fewer than two such cameras flag nothing; excluded cameras are neither
  flagged nor compared with. Used per filter and for `removed`.

**Rules.**
- **The population is the WHOLE session.** Eager: every frame group's
  instances plus its unlinked pool (what `buildTrackerDetections` sees). Lazy:
  every non-resident frame straight from the columnar store via
  `forEachInstanceRow`, with the values hydration would give it (a prediction's
  score `|| 0`, a user row's 0, a node present when x and y are finite), and
  every RESIDENT frame from memory (it may carry edits), never both.
- **Missing scores are reported, not shown as 0%.** Loaders write 0 for an
  absent instance score, and the tracker then drops that detection at any
  minimum above 0 — so "no instance scores" means no prediction has a positive
  score, and the readout says so (overall and per camera) beside the count.
  Missing NODE scores remove nothing, and the readout says that too.

Covered by `tests/test-detection-filter-stats.mjs` (the primitives; eager and
lazy counts against `passesDetectionFilter` over a three-filter sweep of
values, lazy node scores read from the store; missing scores; an empty pool;
excluded cameras; histograms; the flag; the async walk),
`tests/test-tracker-luc3d.mjs` and `tests/test-crossview-populate.mjs` (the
node-score filter keeps a weak animal out of the legacy matcher and the real
`runCrossViewTracker`), `tests/test-single-camera-tracking.mjs` §11 (on one
camera, Track All leaves out exactly what `countDetectionFilters` counts), and
`tests/e2e/detection-filter-preview.mjs`.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js`, `pose/single-camera-tracking.js`,
`ui/detection-filter-preview.js`, `ui/info-panel.js` (`meanNodeScore`, shown
under each instance score in the Instances tab).

---

### pose/skeleton-edit-impact.js

**Purpose.** Count what a skeleton node/edge edit is about to re-shape, so
`ui/skeleton-edit-warning.js` can state it BEFORE anything is mutated. DOM-free
and side-effect-free: it reads the project and returns a tally, and a test pins
that it leaves the skeleton, the 3D, the reprojection cache, the dirty flags and
every instance's node count exactly as it found them.

**Why it exists.** The Skeleton tab's controls look like settings and are not.
`Instance` stores one flat `Float64Array(2N)` keyed by node INDEX, so adding or
removing a node re-shapes every annotation in the project — and LUCID is one
skeleton per project (`setProjectSkeleton` points every session at one object),
so the edit is never local to the session whose panel is open.

**It walks the project exactly as `pose/origin-rebase.js`'s
`countRebaseTargets` does**: the WHOLE 2D population (group members plus
`FrameGroup.unlinkedInstances`), and never `frameGroups` alone on a lazy project
(a small resident window) — a lazy session is counted through
`lazyLoader.forEachInstanceRow`, and `perSession[i].instanceScope` says which
enumeration answered.

**`lazy` is `resident < total`, and it is the headline.** Both propagation
methods are RESIDENT-ONLY by necessity, so frames not in memory come back on the
PREVIOUS skeleton, silently. `residentFrames` / `totalFrames` /
`nonResidentFrames` are what the dialog's red warning quotes. A project with a
`lazyLoader` that is fully resident is NOT flagged: a warning that fires when
nothing is wrong is a warning nobody reads.

**Key exports.**
- `splitSessionsBySkeleton(sessions, skeleton)` → `{shared, others}`. Reference
  identity first (what "one skeleton per project" actually produces), then
  `Skeleton.compatibilityKey()` for a same-shaped copy. A session on a
  DIFFERENT-shaped skeleton is not re-shaped by this edit and goes in `others`,
  so the dialog can say it exists rather than leaving the totals quietly short.
- `countSkeletonEditImpact(sessions)` → the tally: `userInstances`,
  `predictedInstances`, `instances`, `reprojectedInstances`, `groups`,
  `keypoints3d`, the lazy fields above, and `perSession` — the same record once
  per session. Every total is a FOLD of `perSession`, so the dialog's by-session
  rows and its headline cannot be two different arithmetics.
- `skeletonEditNeedsConfirmation(tally)` → the gate. Building the first skeleton
  is N node names typed into a box; a modal per node would make the feature
  unusable, so a project with no instances, no groups and nothing lazy applies
  the edit straight through. `anyLazy` counts on its own, because that is the one
  case where zero counts mean nothing — the annotations are in the file.

**Imports from project modules.** None (deliberately — it is reached from a UI
module but depends on nothing, so it stays Node-testable).

**Imported by.** `ui/skeleton-edit-warning.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-skeleton-edit-impact.mjs` (over REAL
`Session`/`Instance` fixtures) and `tests/e2e/skeleton-edit-warning.mjs` end to
end.

---

### pose/calibration-compare.js

**Purpose.** Do the sessions of a multi-session project agree about where the
world is? A multi-session load reads ONE CALIBRATION PER SESSION FOLDER and
nothing makes them match, so this module answers the question and, when the
answer is no, says which of two very different things went wrong.

**The two disagreements, which need opposite advice.**
- **Same rig, different origin.** `Set as New Calibration` re-bases every
  session in memory but writes ONE file, and `pickCalibrationFile` then PREFERS
  that file on the next load — so one session comes back re-based while the rest
  come back in the original frame. Recoverable by moving a file.
- **Genuinely calibrated apart.** A rig moved, or a camera was re-focused
  between recordings. No file move helps; those sessions' 3D simply is not in a
  shared space.

**Telling them apart is a MEASUREMENT, not a guess.** For one physical camera
described by two calibrations A and B, a world point obeys
`x = R_A*p_A + t_A` and `x = R_B*p_B + t_B`, so if the two worlds are related at
all they are related by `p_A = R_f*p_B + t_f`, giving

    R_f = R_A^T * R_B        t_f = R_A^T * (t_B - t_A)

per camera. If EVERY camera agrees on `(R_f, t_f)` the two calibrations differ
only by where the origin is, and `t_f` is literally the position of B's origin in
A's coordinates (logged to the console, not shown in the modal). If they disagree, no
single frame change explains them. Real re-based copies agree to ~1e-13, so
`FRAME_ROT_SPREAD` / `FRAME_TRANS_SPREAD` can be loose (1e-6, 1e-3 mm).

**Key exports.** `compareSessionCalibrations(sessions)` — groups the sessions by
identical calibration, takes the LARGEST group as the reference (earliest on a
tie, so the wording is stable across reloads) and describes every other group
against it. Each difference gets a `kind`:
- `'cameras'` — a different camera SET, with `onlyHere` / `onlyInReference` names.
- `'lens'` — intrinsics, distortion or image size differ, in `lensCameras`. An
  origin change never touches these, so this alone rules out the recoverable case.
- `'origin'` — one rigid transform explains every camera. Carries `frame` with
  `origin`, `distanceMm`, `angleDeg`, `axis` and the measured `spreadRot` /
  `spreadTransMm`.
- `'extrinsics'` — the cameras moved and no single origin explains them.
`report.frameOnly` is true only when EVERY difference is `'origin'`. The modal
(`ui/calibration-notice.js`) does NOT branch on it — it lists only which
sessions differ; the classification is for the console log and the tests.

**Three deliberate details.** A session with NO calibration — no cameras, or
only placeholders (`hasRealCalibration` false: every camera has all-zero
extrinsics, which is what a folder loaded with no `.toml` now gets) — is excluded
from the comparison and listed in `withoutCalibration` rather than counted as a
third calibration; it would otherwise raise the modal on a perfectly consistent
project. A rig with only its reference camera at the origin is still compared. A camera whose rotation or translation
will not read (NaN, wrong length) is DROPPED, so one malformed entry cannot
masquerade as a divergence. And only cameras whose LENS matches contribute to the
frame solve: a re-focused camera's extrinsics are not a statement about the origin.

**Imports from project modules.** `./origin-frame.js` (`rotationAboutAxis`,
`rotationMatrixToAxisAngle`, `normalize3`) — an `rvec` converts by handing
`rotationAboutAxis` the vector and its own magnitude; `./placeholder-cameras.js`
(`hasRealCalibration`). DOM-free.

**Imported by.** `ui/calibration-notice.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-calibration-compare.mjs` (re-based fixtures built with the
app's own `buildOriginFrame` + `rebaseExtrinsics`, so the detector and the
re-base cannot drift apart silently) and
`tests/e2e/multi-session-calibration-notice.mjs` (the real calibrations off disk).

---

### pose/placeholder-cameras.js

**Purpose.** The stand-in `Camera` a view gets when there is no calibration, and
the rule that every view and every 2D camera key of an UNCALIBRATED session has
one. The `.slp` writer (`buildSlpLabelsAllViews`) writes one video per
`session.cameras` entry and skips any 2D instance whose camera is not in that
list, and the save paths choose the views and videos to write by the same list —
so a session with no cameras saved as an empty file ("Saved (0.0 MB)") and
reopened with no views and no poses. A folder loaded with no `calibration.toml`
was exactly that.

**The placeholder** is the camera File ▸ Load Videos has always made:
`rvec = tvec = [0,0,0]`, no distortion, f = 600, principal point at the image
centre, sized from the video (640×480 when unknown). The app already reads that
shape as "no calibration" (`ui/view-align-modal.js` `isCalibrated`,
`ui/sessions-panes.js` `originHasCalib`, Triangulate's `sessionHasCalibration()`
gate). No loader sets up the 3D viewer for placeholders only (folder loaders,
`handleLoadSlpFile`, `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`), tracking refuses with the
Calibration Required popup, a new group is not auto-triangulated
(`reTriangulateGroup`), and `handleLoadCalibration` replaces them when a real
calibration is loaded.

**Key exports.**
- `makePlaceholderCamera(name, width?, height?)`.
- `isPlaceholderCamera(cam)` — all-zero rotation AND translation (a 3×3
  rotation matrix is calibrated).
- `hasRealCalibration(session)` — any non-placeholder camera. Same rule as
  `pose/triangulation.js` `sessionHasCalibration()` (which takes no argument and
  reads `state.session`); this one takes any session and needs no app state.
- `cameraNamesInUse(session, views?, videoFiles?)` — views (on-screen order),
  then videos (`assignedCamera || name`), then 2D keys: frame-group instances
  and unlinked instances (non-empty lists only), instance-group members, a lazy
  loader's per-camera stores (`labelsByCam`).
- `ensurePlaceholderCameras(session, views?, videoFiles?)` — appends a
  placeholder for each name in use with no camera and assigns each unassigned
  video to the camera named after it; returns the names added. **Does nothing
  to a session with a real calibration**: a stand-in at the world origin would
  join triangulation and corrupt the 3D.
- `PLACEHOLDER_FOCAL`, `PLACEHOLDER_DEFAULT_SIZE`.

**Imports from project modules.** `./pose-data.js` (`Camera`). DOM-free and
state-free (callers pass the session's views and videos), so it loads under Node.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js` (per-camera and single-SLP folder
loaders; `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`'s 3D gate), `import-export/save-load.js`
(`buildSlpBytes`, for every session before writing), `import-export/slp-import.js`
(`handleLoadSlpFile`'s 3D gate), `pose/calibration-compare.js` (placeholder-only
sessions are not compared), `pose/tracker.js` and `ui/track-range-modal.js`
(tracking needs a real calibration).

**Tests.** `tests/test-placeholder-cameras.mjs`;
`tests/e2e/nocalib-folder-save-reopen.mjs` (per-camera folder with no `.toml`:
load → Track All refuses → annotate → Save As → File ▸ Open with the videos;
deferred videos; a hand-built camera-less session; the single-SLP folder loader
and its reopen).

---

### pose/plane-angle.js

**Purpose.** Set the ANGLE BETWEEN two annotated planes. Independently fitted
planes that are perpendicular in reality land a degree or two off, and that
error is baked into everything derived from them; this module lets the user
ASSERT the relationship: hold one plane fixed and rotate the other rigidly until
the angle is met.

DOM-free and THREE-free. It imports **only** `origin-frame.js` and
`plane-data.js`, and deliberately **not** `triangulation.js` (DOM and three.js
UI modules), so it is importable with no loader stubs.

**Hence the optional `fits` argument** on `hingeAxis` and `planAngleEdit`. A
plane can hold 3D for every corner and still carry no stored `planeFit`. The
caller derives one (`ui/plane-angle.js` > `usableFit`, which delegates to the
model's `usablePlaneFit`) and passes it in; the argument is a **test seam** —
`tests/test-plane-angle.mjs` feeds it hand-written fits, including all four
normal-sign combinations. A derived fit and a stored one are treated
identically — `newMovingFit` is the rotation of whichever it got.

**The angle is UNSIGNED, and that is not a simplification.** A fit's normal
sign is arbitrary (only the CONSTRAINED path calls `orientNormalLike`), so a
signed dihedral angle is θ or 180 − θ depending on round-off. The measurement is
`acos(|n_f · n_m|)` ∈ [0°, 90°], invariant under flipping either normal
(`tests/test-plane-angle.mjs` §7).

**The axis is the SHARED EDGE.** Two planes that meet share their corner nodes,
so the line through them is the natural hinge: rotating about it leaves every
shared corner where it was and the fixed plane undisturbed.

| shared, triangulated nodes | axis | `kind` |
|---|---|---|
| 2 or more | the line through the two farthest apart | `'edge'` |
| exactly 1 | through it, along `n_fixed × n_moving` | `'node'` |
| none | through the moving plane's centroid, same direction | `'centroid'` |

`n_fixed × n_moving` is the direction the planes' intersection line runs, so the
fallbacks turn about the direction the shared edge would have had; parallel
normals leave it undetermined and any perpendicular is used (a local `anyPerp`,
matching triangulation.js's own local copy).

**Key exports.**
- `planeAngleDeg(nA, nB)` → degrees in [0, 90], or null. 0 = parallel,
  90 = perpendicular.
- `sharedNodeIds(planeA, planeB)` → the membership intersection in `planeA`'s
  order.
- `hingeAxis(fixedPlane, movingPlane, pool, fits?)` → `{point, dir, kind,
  sharedIds, hingeIds}` or null. `hingeIds` are the corners ON the axis, and so the ones
  guaranteed not to move.
- `sharedNodesSpanAPlane(ids, pool)` — three or more shared corners off a line
  mean the planes already share a PLANE, and no rotation can change their angle
  without moving the fixed plane too. That is `overconstrained`, refused rather
  than approximated.
- `solveAngleRotation(nFixed, nMoving, axisDir, targetDeg)` → radians or null.
  Closed form (up to four candidates); **the smallest |φ| is returned**, which
  keeps a wall tilting the way it already leans rather than flipping through
  the floor — the most load-bearing line in the module. The result is
  re-measured and rejected if it misses the target. `K ≈ 0` means the moving
  normal lies along the hinge, where rotating cannot change the angle.
- `applyRotationToPoints3d(flat, R, pivot, nodeIds, fixedIds)` → a NEW
  `Float64Array`; missing nodes stay missing. `fixedIds` are copied through
  verbatim: a shared corner on the axis drifting by an ulp is exactly the
  divergence the shared node pool exists to prevent.
- `rotatePlaneFit(fit, R, pivot)` — the same rigid motion applied to a stored
  fit. **Rotating rather than re-fitting is deliberate:** a re-fit can flip the
  normal's sign and silently invert anything downstream that read the old one.
  `rms` / `nPoints` ride through unchanged, as does `constrained` when present.
- `planAngleEdit(fixedPlane, movingPlane, targetDeg, model, fits?)` → a plan, mirroring
  `planPlaneFit`. **PURE — it reads the model and writes nothing**, so the UI
  can preview, refuse or cancel with nothing to undo. Refusal codes: `no_model`, `no_plane`,
  `same_plane`, `no_fit`, `bad_target`, `degenerate_axis`, `overconstrained`,
  `unreachable`, and `locked_nodes`.
- `ANGLE_TOL_DEG`, `HINGE_MIN_LEN`, `COLLINEAR_TOL_FRAC`, `NAME_LIST_CAP`.

**Every warning NAMES what it counts** (a bare count is not actionable):
- `plane_locked_elsewhere` lists `node (in "plane", …)` per held node — the
  node FIRST, the planes holding it elsewhere (by MEMBERSHIP) second. A corner
  whose only plane is the moving one rides along and is not reported.
- `stale_fits` lists `"plane" (shares node, …)` — which fit is about to be
  cleared and which moved corner costs it.
- Both are capped at `NAME_LIST_CAP` (3) names with a `+N more` tail; the
  untruncated list is in a `detail` string, which `ui/plane-angle.js` shows as
  a `title` tooltip.
`stalePlaneIds` is unchanged in meaning and shape — the commit path keys off it.

**Pin states decide who may move.** A `locked` node the rotation would move is a
hard refusal (`locked_nodes`, naming the nodes). Nodes on the HINGE are excluded
from that check because they do not move: locking a shared corner is the
documented way to hold an intersection line still, so it must not block the edit
it enables. `plane-locked` nodes are allowed (a rigid rotation keeps them in the
moving plane); one held in a DIFFERENT plane is allowed but warned about, since
the commit projects it back and the result may miss the target.

**Imports from project modules.** `./origin-frame.js` (`normalize3`, `cross3`,
`dot3`, `rotationAboutAxis`, `mulMat3Vec3`), `./plane-data.js`
(`points3dForPlane`).

**Imported by.** `ui/plane-angle.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-plane-angle.mjs` (§10 and §12 pin the warning wording)
and `tests/e2e/plane-angle.mjs` (§6b the rendered warning text and tooltip).

---

### pose/mesh-object-3d.js

**Purpose.** The **grouping layer**: a *3D Mesh Object* is a named set of
PLANES, the way a plane is a named set of nodes. It completes the three-level
model — a node is a point with one 3D position, a plane is a group of nodes, and
an object is a group of planes **whose shape is determined by how those planes
are connected**. Nothing here is geometric; `mesh-object-geometry.js` derives the
shape on demand.

The class is `MeshObject3D` because a JS identifier may not begin with a digit.
**Every user-facing string says "3D Mesh Object"** — the table header, the
buttons, the status messages. Know that before renaming anything.

**Key exports.**
- `class MeshObject3D` — `id` (stable, never reused), `name`, `color` and
  `planeIds` (ordered; insertion order is the face order an export will use).
  Deliberately NO orientation field: winding is derived in
  `pose/mesh-object-geometry.js`, so a stored override could only ever be a
  second source of truth for it. `hasPlane` / `addPlane` / `removePlane`,
  `resolvePlaneIds(model)` / `resolvePlanes(model)` / `planeCount(model)` /
  `danglingCount(model)`.
- `class MeshObjectSet` — `objects`, `size`, `isEmpty`, `createObject(name, {color})`,
  `adoptObject(obj)` (RESTORE path — keeps the file's ID and advances `_nextId`,
  exactly as `PlaneNodePool.adoptNode` does and for the same reason),
  `getObject(id)`, `deleteObject`, `renameObject` (refuses a blank name),
  `recolorObject`, `objectsForPlane(planeId)`, `clear()`.
- `MESH_OBJECT_COLORS`, `defaultMeshObjectColor(i)` — a palette deliberately
  disjoint from `PLANE_COLORS` and `PLANE_NODE_COLORS`. The object's colour is
  its identity in the table swatch and the `.glb`; the 3D selection highlight is
  one fixed yellow (`MESH_MEMBER_COLOR`, `ui/viewport3d.js`), not this colour.

**Why membership resolves LAZILY, and why there is no delete cascade.** Every
read of membership filters against the live planes (`resolvePlaneIds`), so
`PlaneModel.deletePlane` needs no hook and a dangling reference is never
dereferenced. This is what makes the feature **additive**. The cost:
`planeIds.length` is NOT the face count; call `planeCount(model)`.

**Imports from project modules.** None (deliberately dependency-free; the
`PlaneModel` references are JSDoc types only).

**Imported by.** `pose/plane-data.js` (which owns a `MeshObjectSet` as
`PlaneModel.meshObjects` and re-exports these symbols),
`pose/plane-serialization.js`. UI code reaches it through `plane-data.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-mesh-object-3d.mjs` (§4 the additivity guarantee: a
plane model with objects on it is byte-identical to one without, including after
deleting a member plane).

---

### pose/mesh-object-geometry.js

**Purpose.** Turn "a group of planes" into a **mesh**, derived and never stored
— a pure function of the plane model, rebuilt whenever asked. A stored mesh would
go stale the moment a node is dragged, re-triangulated or pinned.

**How connectivity determines the shape.** All three fall out of the global node
pool, and none of it needs a merge-by-distance pass:
1. **Vertices** are the pool nodes the member planes reference — two planes
   referencing one node produce ONE vertex, so a shared corner welds itself.
2. **Faces** are the planes, each a ring of vertex indices.
3. **Edges** are shared automatically: an edge is an unordered pair of vertices,
   and two faces are adjacent iff they list the same pair. That adjacency graph
   IS "how the planes are connected"; shells, naked edges, manifoldness and
   coherent winding are all read off it.

**Key exports.**
- `buildMeshObjectGeometry(obj, model, {frame, scale, eps})` → `MeshObjectGeometry`:
  `vertices` (`Float64Array`, 3V), `vertexNodeIds` (V, provenance), `faces`
  (polygon rings), `facePlaneIds`, `faceNormals` (3F, unit, coherent),
  `triangles` (`Uint32Array`, ear-clipped) and `connectivity` (`vertices`,
  `faces`, `shells`, `edgeCount`, `nakedEdges`, `nonManifoldEdges`, `isClosed`,
  `isOriented`, `degenerateFaces`, `stalledFaces`, `danglingPlaneIds`,
  `coincident`, `volume`).
- `faceRingNodeIds(plane, pool)` — the same dispatch `planeFillOrder3d` uses
  (user edge cycle, else convex hull seeded by `planeFit.normal`) but in NODE
  IDS, which is the only index space two different planes share.
- `faceAdjacency`, `orientFacesCoherently`, `meshConnectivity`, `signedVolume`,
  `areaVectorZ`, `earClip2d`, `earClipFace`, `newellNormal`,
  `coincidentNodeReport`, `connectivitySummary`.

**Winding is the part the annotation does not determine.** A face's ring
depends on `planeFit.normal`, whose SIGN IS ARBITRARY. `orientFacesCoherently`
fixes the relative half by BFS over the adjacency graph (**two faces sharing an
edge traverse it in OPPOSITE directions**). The global half is ONE BIT:

- **CLOSED** — `signedVolume`. Negative ⇒ flip all ⇒ outward.
- **OPEN** — **+Z is up**: `areaVectorZ` (area-weighted vertical component of
  the normal field) must come out non-negative. For a cage the floor alone
  decides, so **the floor faces up** and the walls end up on their INWARD faces.
  It is origin-INDEPENDENT, and "up" is the frame the vertices are already in
  (the user's, with an origin defined). An all-vertical surface has no vertical
  component; `UPRIGHT_EPS` (relative to `surfaceAreaScale`) catches that and
  leaves the coherent pass's orientation alone, which is STABLE across rebuilds
  — reading a sign off ~0 would flip on floating-point noise.

Without this rule the same cage would export either way depending on plane
creation order (`tests/test-mesh-object-geometry.mjs` §3b). **There is no user
override and no `Flip normals` control** — the derivation answers every case.

**Ear clipping, not the viewport's fan.** `viewport3d._buildPlaneFillMesh`'s fan
self-overlaps on a concave face; `earClipFace` clips in the face's own 2D basis.
Its point-in-triangle test is deliberately **non-strict**: a vertex exactly ON a
candidate ear's edge must block it, or the triangle pokes outside the polygon (a
plain L-shape hits this).

**Coordinates.** LUCID's world is Z-up right-handed and **so is Blender's** — no
axis conversion belongs anywhere in this pipeline. The only transforms applied
are the user's origin frame (`applyOriginFrame`) and a uniform positive scale
(non-positive is ignored rather than allowed to mirror the mesh).

**Imports from project modules.** `./plane-data.js` — `planeCycleOrderIds`,
`convexHullOrder3d`; `./origin-frame.js` — `applyOriginFrame`. DOM-free and
THREE-free.

**Imported by.** `ui/mesh-objects.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-mesh-object-geometry.mjs`. Its coherence negative control
checks the **opposite-traversal property directly** rather than comparing
volumes: a face through the world origin contributes zero signed volume however
it is wound, so a unit cube at `[0,1]³` measures +1 even with half its rings
backwards.

---

### pose/pose-data.js

**3D read from a file is marked.** `markPoints3dFromFile(points3d)` /
`isPoints3dFromFile(points3d)` — a `WeakSet` of `points3d` arrays the loaders
read from a file (`import-export/slp-import.js`'s three restore sites and its
points3d `.h5` import). `ui/rendering.js`'s reprojection fill reprojects marked
3D rather than re-solving it, even with a reprojection-error threshold set,
since a re-solve would show LUCID's answer instead of the file's. Keyed by the
ARRAY so it lapses by itself: every path that re-solves or edits a group's 3D
assigns a new array, and nothing but the smoothing pass writes a group's
`points3d` in place. The origin re-base (`applyOriginRebase`) carries the mark
to the re-based array.

**Smoothed 3D is marked the same way.** `markPoints3dSmoothed(points3d, source)` /
`isPoints3dSmoothed(points3d)` — a `WeakMap` from each array
`pose/temporal-smoothing.js` wrote (in place) to a pooled copy of what it held
before its FIRST smoothing (`unsmoothedPoints3d(points3d)`;
`clearPoints3dSmoothed` forgets it). Every later pass filters that copy, so runs
replace each other instead of stacking. The reprojection fill reprojects marked
3D (a re-solve would draw the unsmoothed answer), `adoptPrior3d` never adopts it
(re-triangulating undoes the pass), and the origin re-base re-bases the copy
with the points.

**`InstanceGroup.points3d` on the bulk paths lives in a slab pool.**
`pooledPoints3d(points)` returns a `Float64Array` VIEW of exactly the group's 3N
doubles in a shared 1 MB slab (`isPooledPoints3d` tells them apart; contract in
the source comment). Every bulk writer stores through it: Triangulate All
(`_applyGroupStep`, pose/triangulation.js; `applyIdentitySolve` and Group by
Track, ui/export-modals.js), Track All (`commitTrackedFrame`, pose/tracker.js —
which also stops the group sharing the tracker target's live array), reopen and
the other import paths (import-export/slp-import.js; the JSON project loader in
save-load.js), the origin re-base (`applyOriginRebase`) and
`moveVideosToSession`. Single-frame solves keep their own buffers (bounded). A
view must never be TRANSFERRED or re-wrapped through `.buffer` (that is the
whole slab); nothing does either. Why: one ArrayBuffer per group (~540k after a
large Triangulate All) is swept by every full GC. Covered by
`tests/test-points3d-pool.mjs` and `tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs`
(`checkPooled`).

**`InstanceGroup.reprojectedInstances` starts as the shared
`NO_REPROJECTED_INSTANCES`**, an empty read-only Map (a subclass whose `set`
throws); `addReprojectedInstance` gives the group a Map of its own on its first
write. Reading, iterating, `clear()`, `delete()` and replacing the whole Map by
assignment are fine. Writers must go through `addReprojectedInstance`, test
fixtures included — a direct `.set` on the shared Map throws rather than
leaking an entry into every group.

**`frameIdentityMap` is a `FrameIdentityMap`.** A packed key is above V8's
small-integer range for every frame past 127, so a plain Map cost a heap Number
per entry (millions after Track All) for every full GC to scan.
`FrameIdentityMap extends Map` and overrides every method: numeric keys and
values live in typed arrays in insertion order; other keys (the legacy
`"frame:cam:null"` strings, NaN) and non-number values go to side Maps. Map
semantics are kept exactly — insertion order (load-bearing:
`exportFrameIdentityEntries` writes in it, so the saved bytes depend on it),
`set` on an existing key keeping its place, SameValueZero keys, live iteration.
One difference: when most entries are dead the arrays are COMPACTED, and an
iterator open across a compaction throws rather than skip or repeat silently.
`Session` exposes `frameIdentityMap` through an accessor that converts any plain
Map assigned to it (keeping order), so `= new Map()` still ends up compact. It
must not be structured-cloned or posted to a worker (its Map slot is empty);
nothing does. Covered by `tests/test-frame-identity-map.mjs` (random operations
in lockstep with a real Map).

**`frameIdentityMap` packed keys.** `frameIdentityMap` maps (frameIdx, camera,
raw trackIdx) → identityId with **one entry per 2D detection project-wide**
(~132 MB as string keys on a 2.6M-detection project), so keys are packed into a
single exact-integer `Number`:
`frameIdx * 2^23 + camIdx * 2^17 + (trackIdx + 1)` — 30 bits of frame, 6 of
camera index, 17 of `trackIdx + 1` (so the `-1` untracked sentinel packs as 0),
53 total. Codec: `_fimPack`/`_fimUnpack`/`_fimIsPacked` (module-private) plus
`Session._fimKey`/`_fimDecode`/`_fimCamIdx`.

Every read/write goes through `Session` so the layout lives in one place:
`setFrameIdentity`, `getFrameIdentityValue`, `hasFrameIdentity`,
`deleteFrameIdentity`, and `frameIdentityEntries()` (a generator of decoded
`{frameIdx, camName, trackIdx, identityId, key}` records). **Do not index
`frameIdentityMap` directly**; a raw `"frame:cam:track"` string will simply miss.

Tuples that cannot be packed (trackIdx > 131070, or a camera absent from
`session.cameras`) fall back to the legacy string key rather than silently
losing identities; `_fimDecode` handles both forms.

**Entry format is unchanged.** `exportFrameIdentityEntries()` emits
