`[["frameIdx:camName:trackIdx", identityId], ...]`, and
`ingestFrameIdentityEntries()` accepts BOTH that and packed numbers, so old
projects load. The project JSON (`save-load.js`) writes the full list. The two
`.slp` writers (`file-io.js`, `slp-streaming-write.js`) write only the entries
the instance groups do not imply, through
`import-export/frame-identity-metadata.js`, using:
- `frameIdentityExceptions()` → `{ entries, dropped }`: map entries the groups
  do not reproduce (no group implies the key, a different value, or two groups
  disagree), and keys the groups imply that the map does not hold.
- `_derivedFrameIdentity(frameIdx, cameraName, trackIdx)` — what the groups at
  that frame imply: an identity, `undefined`, or `null` when two disagree. Must
  keep the rule of `stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups` (identity >= 0, track >= 0).
- `applyFrameIdentityEntries(entries)` / `deleteFrameIdentityKeys(keys)` — set /
  delete serialized pairs or keys WITHOUT clearing the map first (both key forms,
  via `_fimNormalizeKey`).
Read side: `save-load.js`, `slp-import.js`, `session-loader.js`. Guarded by the
`frameIdentityMap packed-key codec` block in `tests/test-identity.js`,
`tests/e2e/frame-identity-compact-roundtrip.mjs` (every exception case, both
writers, both readers) and `tests/e2e/save-golden-digest.mjs` at the byte level.

One deliberate exception: `track-identity-ops.js` `deleteTrackAt` still writes the
legacy `"frame:cam:null"` STRING for entries on the deleted track. That is not
representable in the packed space and consumers have always skipped it
(`parseInt('null')` is `NaN`), so packing it would be a behaviour change.

**`points3d` flat typed arrays.**
`InstanceGroup.points3d` is a flat **`Float64Array(3 * nNodes)`** — node `k` at
`[3k, 3k+1, 3k+2]`. A missing / un-triangulated node is an **all-NaN triple**,
not a `null` row. Why: boxed rows live in V8's pointer-compressed heap, which a
Chrome renderer hard-caps near 4 GB (~410 MB of it on a 531k-group project); a
typed array's backing store is allocated OUTSIDE that cap.

**f64, not f32, is deliberate:** it costs the *same* in the cage (only the
external backing store doubles), and keeps every coordinate bit-identical, which
is what keeps `tests/e2e/save-golden-digest.mjs` byte-for-byte unchanged.

Codec (exported here, used everywhere): `POINT3D_STRIDE` (3), `makePoints3d`, `points3dNodeCount`,
`hasPoint3d`, `getPoint3d`, `readPoint3d` (allocation-free), `setPoint3d`,
`clearPoint3d`, `someValidPoint3d`, `countPoints3d`, `clonePoints3d`,
`toBoxedPoints3d`, `fromBoxedPoints3d`, `asPoints3d` (dual-format ingest —
passes a `Float64Array` through UNCOPIED, converts boxed rows).
**Do not index `points3d` directly**; `pts[k]` is a coordinate, not a point.

Collapsing `null` into NaN loses no information: the SLP format has always
written NaN for missing 3D keypoints. `null` is still accepted on the way in
(`fromBoxedPoints3d`, `setPoint3d`) and still emitted at the boundaries that need
the legacy shape — the JSON project format (`save-load.js`) and the
`points3d.h5` export (`file-io.js`) both go through `toBoxedPoints3d`.

**`observedPoints` is derived, not stored.**
`InstanceGroup.observedPoints` — `{cameraName: inst.toPointsArray()}` per member —
is a **getter over `instances`**, so it is in sync with the members by
construction. There is **no setter**: a stray assignment throws in the app
(strict mode) and is silently discarded in the classic-script test harness —
either way it cannot reinstate a stored copy that drifts from `instances` (see
`tests/test-edit-group-fixes.js`). It **allocates a fresh object per access** —
hoist it into a local before reading per-camera in a loop; the per-frame overlay
draw goes straight to `group.getInstance(view).points` instead.
`purgeTriangulationDataForGroup` does not null it: every consumer gates on
`points3d && reprojections`, which the purge still clears. The JSON project
format still WRITES it for backward compat, but ignores it on restore.

**`Instance` flat coordinate storage.** `Instance` holds 2D keypoints in a flat
**`Float64Array(2 * nNodes)`** (`_xy`, node `k` at `[2k, 2k+1]`, **NaN x = no
point**) and occlusion in a **bit set** (`_occ`: a plain Number at <=32 nodes,
else a `Uint32Array`) — ~824 → 172 B of V8 heap per instance, for millions of
instances.

**`points` and `occluded` are DELETED, not kept as getters.** `inst.points.length`
meant *nNodes* and would silently double on a `Float64Array(2n)`, and
`inst.occluded[k]` against a Number bitmask yields `undefined` (falsy), which
would silently drop occlusion from every export. Removing the fields turns both
into an immediate `TypeError`.

Accessors: `numNodes`, `hasPoint`, `getX`/`getY`, `getPoint` (allocates),
`readPoint(k, out)` (allocation-free), `setPoint`, `setPointFrom`, `clearPoint`,
`isOccluded`, `setOccluded`, `anyOccluded`, `countPoints`, `hasAnyPoint`,
`hasAnyUsablePoint`, `toPointsArray`/`toOccludedArray` (serialization),
`setPointsFrom`/`setOccludedFrom`, `adoptPointsFrom` (deliberate buffer sharing,
for lazy-2D hydration), `insertNodeAt`/`removeNodeAt` (skeleton edits — these
resize any backup alongside, so `restorePoints()` stays node-aligned),
`backupPoints`/`restorePoints`/`hasBackup`.

**The constructor** still takes boxed `[[x,y]|null, ...]` (or a
`Float64Array`, adopted by reference) and normalizes. A boxed `[NaN, NaN]` row
reads as no point.

**The shared lazy placeholder.** `lazyPlaceholderXY(numNodes)` returns ONE
NaN-filled `Float64Array(2n)` per node count, and `isLazyPlaceholderXY(xy)`
recognises it by identity. Every lazy `InstanceGroup` member whose 2D lives only
in the store points at it: a reopened project's placeholders
(`reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy`) and members whose frame went
non-resident (`releaseFrameMembers2d`, `pose/lazy-residency.js`). **It is never
written:** the three IN-PLACE writers — `setPoint`, `clearPoint` and
`setPointVisible`'s restore — call the private `_ownXY()` first, which swaps in a
copy when `_xy` is the placeholder. Writers that assign a new array need no
guard. A NEW in-place writer must call `_ownXY()`, or one edit moves every
lightweight member in the project.

f64 rather than f32 is deliberate: identical cage cost, and bit-exact values keep
`tests/e2e/save-golden-digest.mjs` byte-for-byte unchanged.

**Per-node model scores live in ONE pool, not on each instance.** A prediction's
`pred_points.score` per node (shown by `ui/node-tooltip.js`, written back by the
writers) is copied by `Instance.setPointScores(src, start)` into module-private
`Float32Array` chunks (`storePointScores`); the instance keeps only `_ps`, the
integer offset of its first node (-1 = none, set in the constructor so every
Instance keeps one shape). A `Float32Array` per instance would be ~320 B of heap
plus an ArrayBuffer per instance, on millions of predictions. Segments are
written once and never edited, so `adoptPointScoresFrom` may share one and
`insertNodeAt`/`removeNodeAt` write a new segment (`splicePointScores`); nothing
is freed. `pointScore(k)` is NaN for no score and for any `k` that is not an
integer in range. **A lazy project does not use the pool**: frames re-hydrate on
every revisit and would refill it, so its scores stay in the store (below).

**Purpose.** Pure data-model classes — no DOM, no I/O. The single source of
truth for skeletons, cameras, instances, frame groups, identities, and the
session graph that holds them.

**Key exports.**
- `Skeleton` — node names + edge list. Methods: `addNode`, `removeNode`,
  `addEdge`, `removeEdge`, `clone()` (deep copy; used to cache/seed a
  remembered skeleton without aliasing a live session),
  `compatibilityKey()` (order-independent canonical string of node names + edges
  as unordered name pairs; two skeletons share a key iff an instance copied from
  one can be pasted onto the other — see instance copy/paste in `ui-wiring.js`),
  static `defaultMouse()`.
- `Camera` — intrinsics (`matrix`), distortion, rvec/tvec, image size.
  Cached getters `rotationMatrix`, `extrinsicMatrix`, `projectionMatrix`;
  methods `project`, `projectPoints` (ideal pinhole, no distortion),
  `undistortPoint` (distorted→ideal, iterative), and `distortPoint`
  (ideal→distorted, OpenCV forward model, used to re-distort reprojections into
  native pixel space).
  **`setExtrinsics(rvec, tvec)`** replaces the extrinsics in place and drops the
  memoized matrices — there is no other way to invalidate them, so assigning
  `rvec`/`tvec` directly leaves a camera that REPORTS its new pose and PROJECTS
  with its old one. It mutates rather than replacing the object because callers
  hold `Camera` references obtained by name lookup. Pinned by
  `tests/test-origin-rebase.mjs` §7.
- `Instance` — per-view 2D keypoints (flat `_xy`, see above) with `trackIdx`,
  `type` (`user`/`predicted`/`reprojected`), `score`, occlusion bit set `_occ`,
  `nulledNodes`,
  and `identityId` (null unless this is a TRACKLESS UNLINKED instance whose
  disbanded group's identity was retained on it by `unlinkGroup`;
  consumed/cleared when the instance joins a group; not persisted).
  Methods `toggleOccluded`, `setPointVisible`, `backupPoints`, `restorePoints`,
  and the per-node model scores `setPointScores(src, start)`,
  `adoptPointScoresFrom(other)`, `hasPointScores()`, `pointScore(k)` (see the
  score pool above).
- `UnlinkedInstance` — wrapper around an `Instance` not yet placed in an
  `InstanceGroup`. Auto-incrementing `id`.
- `FrameGroup` — per-frame container of linked `instances` and
  `unlinkedInstances`, both keyed by camera name.
- `Identity` — id + name + color (uses `IDENTITY_COLORS` palette). A color
  passed in that equals the OLD default for its slot (`id % 20`, private
  `LEGACY_IDENTITY_COLORS`) is replaced by the current default; any other color
  is kept. Every load path builds identities through this constructor, so this
  is the one upgrade point.
- `IDENTITY_COLORS` — 20-color palette; identity N takes entry N. Entries 0-6
  are the original neon set and must not change; 7-19 were chosen farthest-first
  (CIEDE2000). `UNIDENTIFIED_TRACK_COLORS` (`ui/overlays.js`) must stay
  ΔE2000 ≥ 20 from every entry. Pinned by
  `tests/test-track-identity-palette.mjs` §2, §8 and §9, so a change here
  re-runs that test.
- `InstanceGroup` — cross-view grouped instances + triangulated `points3d`
  + cached `reprojectedInstances`. `markDirty`/`markClean`. Also
  `triangulationMethod` (`'ba'`|`'dlt'`|undefined), recording WHICH SOLVER
  produced `points3d`. Read by `resolveTriangulationMethod`,
  `ui/rendering.js`'s lazy reprojection fill, `adoptPrior3d` and the Info Panel's
  method label — and **persisted**, in per-group
  `metadata.lucid.triangulationMethod` (written only when `'ba'`; absent means
  DLT), because it cannot be reconstructed from `points_3d` — without it a
  reopened BA project's display fill would re-derive reprojections with DLT and
  report DLT's error for BA points. Guarded by
  `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-ba-file-roundtrip.mjs`.
- `groupDisplayName(session, group, frameIdx)` — the name a status line gives an
  `InstanceGroup`, never `undefined`/`null`. A group has **no `trackIdx`** (its
  members do, possibly `null`), and `identityId` is not an index into
  `session.tracks`, so neither may be read as a track index. Resolved in
  `getGroupColor`'s order: the per-frame identity of a member's own
  (camera, trackIdx), then `group.identityId` via `getIdentity`, then the first
  member's track name (`'Track N'` for an unnamed index), then `'group'`. Only a
  non-negative integer `trackIdx` counts as a track (a legacy `-1` is
  trackless). Used by the five group-naming status lines in
  `pose/initialization.js`. Tested by `tests/test-group-display-name.mjs`.
- `Session` — top-level container: cameras, skeleton, tracks, identities,
  frameGroups, instanceGroups.
  - **`fileProvenance`** — the provenance of the `.slp` it was loaded from,
    without LUC3D's own keys (null when none); in memory only, written back on
    save by `import-export/slp-provenance.js`.
  - **`slpVideoByCamera`** — camera name → the video path the `.slp` gave for
    it, set by File ▸ Load SLP (`handleLoadSlpFile`, `handleAddSlp`); in memory
    only, never saved. Lets a second Load SLP of the same video be recognised
    (`camerasAlreadyInSession`, `import-export/slp-merge.js`).
  - **`predictedNodeScore(cameraName, frameIdx, inst, k)`** is the one answer to
    "what did the model score this node?" — the node tooltip and the streaming
    writer's overlay both ask it. NaN unless `inst` is `'predicted'` (an edit
    makes an instance `'user'`; a reprojection was never scored). An eager
    prediction answers from its own scores; a lazy one from the store row it was
    hydrated from (`_rawInstIndex`, which must be an integer — `null` is not
    row 0), via `lazyLoader.predictedPointScore` and by node NAME, so a skeleton
    edit after loading cannot pair a node with another node's score.
  - **`numAnimals`** — the number of animals every tracking run on the session
    caps identities at: a positive integer, `'auto'`, or `null` while nobody has
    been asked, which is what makes Track Frame / Track All prompt. Saved as
    `metadata.lucid.numAnimals` (`import-export/visibility-metadata.js`), read
    and set through `pose/tracker.js`.
  - `numFrames` returns `lazyLoader.nFrames` on a lazy session (`frameGroups`
    there holds only the resident window) and `frameGroups.size` otherwise.
  - **Tracks and identities are per-session.** The constructor copies the
    incoming `tracks` array so two sessions never share one. **Identity is
    stored ONLY per-frame** in `frameIdentityMap` (packed keys, above; negative
    = explicit "no identity"). There is deliberately no global "cam:trackIdx"
    default map — a global fallback painted stale duplicate identities whenever
    per-frame reality diverged from it.
  - **Per-frame assignment:** `setFrameIdentity`; `assignTrackToIdentity`
    (resident-only, and has **no callers** — left alone deliberately);
    `clearTrackIdentity`; `propagateIdentity` — stamps an identity from a start
    frame FORWARD, **project-wide on a lazy session**: a resident pass over
    `frameGroups` (authoritative — it sees in-memory edits and unlinked
    instances) then a `lazyLoader.forEachInstanceRow` pass over the store for
    every frame the first pass could not see, both applying the same
    per-frame uniqueness rule (`_applyIdentityAtFrame`). It follows ONE raw
    `(camera, trackIdx)` pair, so it dies at the first fragment boundary of that
    raw track; it is only the FALLBACK for a manual switch when there is no
    identity to swap away from.
  - **`swapIdentitiesForward(startFrame, identityA, identityB)`** — what a MANUAL
    identity correction means: **exchanges two identities from `startFrame`
    through the END of the project, in every view** (the identity-layer
    analogue of SLEAP's `Labels.track_swap`). Works over identity VALUES, which
    are dense project-wide, so it needs no raw-track continuity — real
    per-camera tracker output fragments constantly. Rewrites both durable
    structures: `frameIdentityMap` (values only; the frame filter is arithmetic
    on the packed key) and the whole-project `instanceGroups[*].identityId`
    (saved, used as the display fallback, and read by
    `propagateIdentitiesToTracks` step 2b — leaving it stale would let a later
    IDs→Tracks resurrect the pre-swap assignment). Frames before `startFrame`
    are never touched, and the swap is an involution, so a later correction
    composes to "until the next manual correction". No frame materialization.
    Returns `{entries, groups, frames}`. It is
    `swapIdentitiesInRange(startFrame, Infinity, …)`. Guarded by the
    `swapIdentitiesForward (#172)` block in `tests/test-identity.js` and
    `tests/e2e/identity-switch-propagates-to-end.mjs`.
  - `swapIdentitiesInRange(startFrame, endFrame, identityA, identityB)` — the
    same swap bounded on BOTH sides (inclusive), what fixing a flagged ID switch
    does (`ui/id-switch-modal.js`). Its own inverse, which is how a fix is
    undone. Covered by `tests/test-id-switch-fix.mjs` §3.
  - `swapIdentitiesForwardInCamera(startFrame, cameraName, identityA,
    identityB)` — the **single-view** counterpart (ungroup → switch the ID on
    that view's Ungrouped row → regroup). It deliberately does NOT rewrite
    `instanceGroups[*].identityId`, a single field shared by every view, which
    would leak the correction into the other cameras (`groups` in the result is
    always 0, kept so the shape matches for `describeIdentitySwitch`). Guarded
    by `tests/test-ungroup-retains-identity.mjs` and
    `tests/e2e/ungroup-retains-identity.mjs`.
  - Group assignment (`assignIdentityToGroup(group, identityId,
    hostFrameIdx?)` — without the hint it scans ALL of `instanceGroups` to find
    its host frame, so a caller already iterating frames must pass it; the
    unhinted form froze the tab in `propagateTracksToIdentities`' align pass.
    Single-group interactive callers omit it), lookup
    (`getIdentityIdForTrack`/`getIdentityForTrack` — per-frame only, null with
    no fallback; `isExplicitNoIdentity`; `isNoIdTrack(trackIdx)` — true for the
    dedicated `NO_ID_TRACK_NAME` ("No ID") track, colored `NULL_ID_COLOR`),
    `getOrCreateIdentityForTrack` (creates/returns the "id_N" identity only —
    no map side effects; a LINEAR scan, so the lazy sweep below memoizes it per
    `trackIdx`).
  - **Identity↔track propagation.** `propagateTracksToIdentities` (Tracks→IDs)
    stamps each instance's per-frame identity from its track; on a lazy session
    it also sweeps `lazyLoader.forEachInstanceRow` for instances outside the
    resident window. `propagateIdentitiesToTracks` (IDs→Tracks) overwrites each
    instance's `trackIdx` with its identity and rewrites `tracks` to one unique,
    non-empty name per used identity, rewriting `frameIdentityMap` under the new
    keys; instances with no identity (entry-less OR the negative sentinel)
    become trackless (`trackIdx = null`) — no "No ID" track is created. Returns
    `{tracks, instances, lazyErrorRows, ambiguousRawKeys}`. Its rules:
    - **Whole-project on a lazy session.** The used-identity set and the
      remapped map are derived from `frameIdentityMap` itself (always complete) —
      a map built from a `frameGroups` walk would destroy identity data for every
      non-resident frame when it replaces the map. `frameGroups` is still walked
      to keep resident `trackIdx` live, and `lazyLoader.remapTracksFromIdentity`
      rewrites the store's track column (no frame materialization) through
      `oldKeyToNewTrackIdx`, a packed-key `FrameIdentityMap` built in step 2.
      Both lazy delegations are duck-typed (`typeof … === 'function'`), so a
      non-lazy session or the worker-backed `LazyFrameLoader` is unaffected.
    - **Step 3b remaps `session.instanceGroups` project-wide too.** On a lazy
      session those members are separate lightweight objects from `frameGroups`
      (`reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy`) and `finalizeLazyFrameGroup`
      never refreshes their `trackIdx`; left stale they show old colors in 3D
      (`getGroupColor`), blank the Info panel's track `<select>` and overlay old
      bars in the Timeline (`_buildTrackSegments`). `ui/ui-wiring.js`'s
      propagate handlers call `update3DViewport` so 3D recolors at once.
    - **Raw-trackIdx collisions.** `commitTrackedFrame` marks a
      (frame, cam, rawTrackIdx) key `-1`/ambiguous when the raw tracker gives two
      animals the SAME trackIdx on one frame (most common on frame 0). Resolving
      through that key alone would leave both trackless — in memory and, since an
      absent key in `oldKeyToNewTrackIdx` means *no track*, in the STORE. So
      step 2b builds **`rowClaim`**, keyed `(frame, camera, offsetInFrame)` from
      each group member's `_rawInstIndex`, which the store-remap callback
      consults FIRST (hence the callback's `offsetInFrame` argument, see
      `loading/sio-lazy-loader.js`); a per-track `rawClaim` fallback covers a
      member with no `_rawInstIndex` and refuses to guess when two identities
      contest one key — counted as **`ambiguousRawKeys`**, `console.warn`ed and
      shown by `ui/ui-wiring.js`'s status line. Step 2b also supplements
      `newFrameMap` from `group.identityId` (so `_buildIdentitySegments` sees
      it), and an `instanceToIdentity` Map is consulted by `remapInstance`.
    - **Precedence: the map FIRST, `group.identityId` only as a fallback**
      (entry absent or `-1`). That matches every display consumer
      (`getGroupColor` is map-first) and matters because
      `swapIdentitiesForwardInCamera` rewrites ONLY the map — group-first would
      resurrect the stale group identity and put the SAME ID on both animals on
      the switch frame.
    - **`remapInstance` runs once per instance** (a `remapped` Set): an object
      shared by the step-3 and step-3b walks must not be remapped twice, since
      the second pass would look its rewritten `trackIdx` up in the old map and
      undo the first.
    - The auto-generated track name is `'track_' + index` (a custom identity
      name is preserved; only a placeholder `id_<n>` is replaced), so
      Tracks→IDs→Tracks restores the original naming.
    Guarded by `propagateIdentitiesToTracks run twice (luc3d #203)` in
    `tests/test-lazy-reopen.js` (incl. the invariant that the Timeline's
    `maxTrackIdx + 1` equals `session.tracks.length`),
    `tests/e2e/first-frame-track-identity-collision.mjs`, and the
    `single-view ID switch (duplicate-ID regression)` block in
    `tests/test-pose-data.js`.
  - Legacy migration (`migrateGlobalIdentitiesToPerFrame` — converts a
    pre-per-frame project's global map on load).
  - **Group editing.** `createGroupFromUnlinked` — when no identity is passed it
    prefers an identity the members ALREADY read as (first member with one, via
    `getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance`), then the first member's track if it has
    one; identity-less trackless members give a group with NO identity (-1), not
    a fabricated "id_null". Preferring the held identity is what lets ungroup →
    re-assign one row → regroup keep the animal's ID. Grouping CONSUMES the
    members' instance-level identity (`Instance.identityId` → null;
    `assignToGroup` does the same).
    `unlinkGroup` — **retains identity** for each member before the group (and
    the `group.identityId` fallback) is dropped (`_retainIdentityOnUnlink`): a
    TRACKED member gets it stamped into `frameIdentityMap`; a TRACKLESS member
    gets it on the instance (`Instance.identityId`), since a null track keys one
    shared per-camera slot. The map stamp never overwrites an existing positive
    entry and skips a raw-trackIdx key still shared with another group in the
    frame (the ambiguous-`-1` case). The instance stamp OVERWRITES: while
    grouped, `group.identityId` is the freshest truth for a trackless member.
    `getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance(cam, instance, frameIdx)` — THE resolver
    for an UNLINKED instance's identity: the per-frame map entry when it has a
    track, else the instance-level retained identity. Used by the info panel's
    Ungrouped rows, `getInstanceColor`, and the regroup derivation.
    `assignIdentityToUnlinkedTrackless(frameIdx, camName, instance, identityId)`
    — the one-view ID correction for a TRACKLESS unlinked row; per-frame by
    nature, and hands the vacated identity to the trackless unlinked row in the
    same camera that held the target, keeping the view duplicate-free. Routed to
    by `applyIdentitySwitch`. Guarded by
    `tests/test-ungroup-retains-identity.mjs` and
    `tests/e2e/ungroup-trackless-reopen.mjs`.
    Also `removeInstanceGroup`, `assignToGroup`.
  - Repair (`deduplicateFrameIdentities`, `scrubOrphanInstances`,
    `_promoteIfMixed`), skeleton propagation
    (`propagateNodeAdded`/`propagateNodeRemoved`), camera-rename
    (`renameCameraInAllData`).

  **Skeleton propagation re-shapes the 2D, the `nulledNodes` sets and the 3D
  together**, because all three are indexed by the same node index and anything
  that drifts re-seats every node past the edit onto its neighbour:
  - `propagateNodeAdded(opts)` — appends a slot to every instance.
    **`opts.hidden`** (what `ui/info-panel.js` passes) places the node beside the
    instance's placed points and adds it to `nulledNodes`, so it draws a grey,
    CLICKABLE marker — an empty slot draws nothing and could never be clicked.
    **PREDICTED instances are left empty**: nothing is invented for model
    output; `_convertToUserInstance` fills them when they become editable.
  - `propagateNodeRemoved(nodeIdx)` — splices the node out of every instance,
    re-seats `nulledNodes` above it, and **splices `InstanceGroup.points3d`**
    rather than discarding it; the group is marked dirty.
  - Both drop the group's cached `reprojections` / `reprojectedInstances` (per-node
    at the OLD count), handing the latter back to `NO_REPROJECTED_INSTANCES`, and
    re-shape `points3d` through **`pooledPoints3d`** — they walk every group, a
    bulk path, so own buffers would un-pool the whole project's 3D.
  - Both are **RESIDENT-ONLY by necessity** (`_warnResidentOnlyStructuralEdit`):
    the columnar store has a fixed node count per instance, so a non-resident
    frame comes back on the previous skeleton. `pose/skeleton-edit-impact.js`
    measures that and `ui/skeleton-edit-warning.js` warns before the edit.
  **`videoContrast`** / **`videoBrightness`** / **`videoRotation`** — three
  `{ cameraName: int }` maps: contrast in [−100, 100], brightness percentage in
  [0, 200], rotation degrees in [−179, 180]. **Per-session** and persisted in the
  `.slp` (`metadata.lucid.*`): `state.views` is rebuilt on every session switch,
  so a per-view field would silently reset. Contrast and brightness are
  display-only (CSS filters); rotation is not — the renderer and hit-testing read
  `view.rotation`, which `restoreViewRotation` re-seeds from here at pane build.
  Defaults (0 / 100 / 0) are never stored — see `ui/video-filters.js`, which owns
  every read/write, and `import-export/visibility-metadata.js`.
  The timeline's `_hiddenCameras` / `_hiddenTracks` / `_hiddenIdentities` Sets
  are session-scoped and persisted the same way (see
  `ui/timeline-visibility.js`).
- `clonePoints(points)` — deep-clone helper for `[u,v]|null` arrays.
- `mat3x3Multiply`, `mat3x3Multiply3x4` — matrix utilities used by
  `Camera` and `triangulation.js`.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `demo-data.js`; `pose/` — `cross-view-tracker.js`,
`id-switch-check.js`, `initialization.js`, `lazy-residency.js`,
`origin-rebase.js`, `placeholder-cameras.js`, `plane-data.js`, `plane-fit.js`,
`tracker.js`, `tracker-worker.js`, `triangulation.js`, `triangulation-core.js`,
`triangulation-pool.js`, `triangulation-worker.js`; `import-export/` —
`file-io.js`, `save-load.js`, `skeleton-json.js`, `slp-import.js`,
`slp-merge.js`, `slp-streaming-write.js`; `loading/session-loader.js`; `ui/` —
`export-modals.js`, `identity-assignment.js`, `info-panel.js`, `interaction.js`,
`origin-definition.js`, `plane-definition.js`, `plane-list-panel.js`,
`rendering.js`, `sessions-panes.js`, `timeline.js`, `ui-wiring.js`,
`viewport3d.js`.

**User-facing features.** Underpins everything: skeleton editing, instance
manipulation, identity assignment, camera projection, multi-track
bookkeeping, session save/load.

---

### pose/tracker-worker.js

**Purpose.** Module worker that runs Track All's `CrossViewTracker` off the
main thread, spawned by `pose/tracker-pool.js`. The tracker is
sequential — each frame is matched against the targets the previous one left —
so ONE worker runs it, frame by frame, in order; what moves off the main thread
is the work itself, while the main thread keeps hydrating frames and committing
the results.

**Bit-identical by construction.** It runs the same code
(`pose/cross-view-tracker.js`) on the same inputs in the same order:
- cameras arrive with the main thread's own cached `_cachedR` / `_cachedRt` /
  `_cachedP` installed, so no matrix is re-derived here;
- each detection arrives as a copy of its instance's flat `_xy` — the only
  thing `Detection` reads (`toPointsArray` → `getPoint`), and it snapshots that
  at construction, so the tracker never reads an `Instance` again — in the same
  per-camera slot order `buildTrackerDetections` uses (the detection filter is
  applied on the main thread, by `buildTrackerInputs`).
Pinned by `tests/e2e/track-all-worker.mjs`.

**Message protocol.** A batch travels as a handful of TYPED ARRAYS each way,
never one object or buffer per detection — per-detection serialization made the
worker run 10% SLOWER than inline.
- IN `{type: 'init', hp, cameras: [{name, matrix, dist, rvec, tvec, size, R, Rt, P}]}`
  → `{type: 'ready'}`. The arrays are rebuilt by push (`unboxed`): a structured
  clone delivers V8's generic boxed-number arrays, and the tracker reads these
  matrices in its innermost loops.
- IN `{type: 'frames', id, fis, counts, lens, xy}` — frame `i`'s camera `c` has
  `counts[i*C + c]` detections, each one's `_xy` the next `lens[d]` values of
  `xy` (frame, camera, slot order); a detection's `Instance` adopts its VIEW.
  → `{type: 'tracked', id, r: {fis, nT, live, trackIds, nMem, mem, p3len, p3}}`
  — per frame, what `commitTrackedFrame` reads, in tracker order: `nT[i]`
  targets holding a detection from >= 2 cameras THIS frame, each with its
  `trackId`, `nMem` (camera index, slot) pairs in `mem` in its `detsByCam` order,
  and a copy of its 3D as the next `p3len` values of `p3` (-1: none); `live[i]`
  = the tracker's target count after the frame (the run's `numTargets`). All
  transferred.
- Any throw → `{type: 'error', id, message}`.

**Imports from project modules.** `./pose-data.js` (`Camera`, `Instance`),
`./cross-view-tracker.js` (`CrossViewTracker`, `Detection`) — both worker-safe.

**Imported by.** Spawned by `pose/tracker-pool.js` via
`new Worker(new URL('pose/tracker-worker.js', document.baseURI), {type: 'module'})`.

**User-facing features.** Track All / Track Frame Range run their tracker here.

---

### pose/tracker-pool.js

**Purpose.** The main-thread side of Track All's tracker worker
(`pose/tracker-worker.js`): `startTrackerWorker(cameras, hp, onFrame, opts)`
spawns it, posts `init`, and resolves with a handle once it answers `ready` — or
`null` (track inline) when workers are unavailable, `window.LUCID_TRACKER_WORKER
=== 0` (`trackerWorkerWanted`; the kill switch and the A/B tests' inline side),
or it fails to start. A worker that cannot start never costs a run.

**Handle.** `submit(fi, cams)` queues one frame (per camera, in the run's
order, each detection's flat 2D in slot order), COPYING it into the batch's one
growing `Float64Array`, and posts the batch (transferred) every `TRACKER_BATCH`
(100) frames. `saturated()` / `throttle()` hold the main
thread to at most `TRACKER_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` (20) batches ahead — 2,000 frames, one
sweep window, so the worker has a window's work queued while the main thread
hydrates the next instead of idling through it. `committedThrough(fi)` resolves
once every submitted frame up to `fi` is committed (flushing a partial batch);
`finish()` once everything is. `cpuBusyMs()` is the worker's busy time (a
`createBusyClock` lane open while it holds a batch) for the CPU line; `close()`
terminates it.

**Results commit IN ORDER, from the message handler.** `decode` turns a batch's
`r` back into `onFrame(fi, targets, live)` calls — `targets`
`[{trackId, points3d, members}]`, `members` a view of (camera index, slot) pairs
and `points3d` a view to COPY (`pooledPoints3d` does), not keep — for each frame
of each returned batch, wrapped in
`opts.busyDuring` so the loading overlay counts it as main-thread work even
inside an `idleWhile` wait. A frame must still be RESIDENT when its result
arrives — the caller holds sweep windows until `committedThrough`. A commit that
throws, or a worker that errors, sets `failure`: every later wait rejects with
it, so a mid-run failure fails the pass (error status) rather than hanging.

**Imports from project modules.** `../ui/load-graph.js` (`createBusyClock`).

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js`.

**User-facing features.** Track All / Track Frame Range speed, and their CPU
line (the worker plus the main thread).

---

### pose/tracker.js

**Purpose.** Cross-view instance matching and identity assignment. Pairwise
epipolar/reprojection scoring, Hungarian assignment, multi-frame
identity propagation.

**One camera: SLEAP's tracker.** A session with a single camera (a plain SLEAP
predictions file opened with Load SLP) has nothing to match across views and no
3D, so `runTrackingPass` hands Track All to `runSingleCameraTrackAll`, which
runs sleap-nn's tracker (`pose/sleap-tracker.js`, via
`pose/single-camera-tracking.js`) with the animal count as its track cap (the
session's `numAnimals`, asked once and shown in the toolbar's Animals field, as for the
multi-camera pass) and the
Tracking Wizard's `scWindowSize` / `scOksStddev` / `scConnectBreaks`, ends on the
Track All summary box planned for one camera (`ui/track-summary-modal.js`; timed
from the tracker call, counted on the single-camera stand-in), and with
the Tracking Wizard's detection filters (`filterMinVisibleNodes` /
`filterMinInstanceScore` / `filterMinMeanNodeScore`, read as the multi-camera
pass reads them; default 0 = off) leaves the detections that fail them out
first, and with `scRemoveDuplicates` on (default off) then each frame's duplicate
detections (`scDuplicateIou` / `scDuplicateOks`); the status line and the summary
box count each apart ("removed by the detection filters", "duplicate … removed
first"). It writes
the TRACKS, as `sleap-nn track` does, plus one identity per track, then follows
the multi-camera pass's contract: `markDirty()` after the last bail-out, the
previous identities and ID-switch results cleared, Color: ID, predictions only,
Timeline and 3D viewer closed, and the automatic coat-brightness check
(`autoBrightnessSwitchCheck`, default on — no model, no GPU) and image check
(`autoImageSwitchCheck`) when they are on. It
keeps Track All's candidate moments on the session (`session._idSwitchCandidates`,
cleared at the start of the run) for the checks to test, and puts its REVIEW LIST in
the ID Switches tab before the checks run (`setReviewList` with
`pose/single-camera-tracking.js` `reviewResult` — the moments plus handovers, as
unscored rows; with two or more tracks), counting it in the status line ("N places
to review in the ID Switches tab") and the summary box (`reviewPlaces`).
The body-size check is OPTIONAL on one camera (the user's choice, 2026-10-12):
Track All does not run it, the status line and the summary box say so
(`SINGLE_CAMERA_SIZE_SKIP_NOTE`), and the menu runs it. Its 2D accuracy is under
`pose/single-camera-tracking.js`. Track Frame and Track Frame Range refuse
on one camera, saying why, as does Track All on a lazy one-camera project.
~1 s for an 18,000-frame, 3-mouse video in the app.

**Track All closes the Timeline and the 3D viewer.** On success, a full Track
All calls `collapseTimeline()` (`ui/timeline-controller.js`) and
`collapseViewport3D(viewport3d)` (`ui/panel-visibility.js`) — there is no 3D pose
to look at until Triangulate All runs. A closed panel stays closed. Track Frame
Range and Track Frame leave both as they were. Covered by
`tests/e2e/track-all-closes-timeline-and-3d.mjs`.

**Track All ends on a summary box.** After the automatic ID-switch checks,
`runTrackingPass` (Track All only — a range is a targeted re-run inspected on the
timeline) opens `showTrackSummaryModal` (`ui/track-summary-modal.js`) with
`summarizeTrackedIdentities` and one `describeSwitchCheck` per cue
(`ui/track-summary.js`), after keeping both on the session with
`rememberTrackSummary`, so the ID Switches tab can reopen the box once it is
closed. It reports how long tracking took — `trackStart` is taken AFTER the
first-use dialog, so the user's typing is not counted, and stops when the
identity pass returns — with its speed (fps, and × real time from `state.fps`,
falling back to `session.fps`), and each check's own `elapsedMs` (stamped by
`runIdSwitchChecks`). The summary is wrapped in its own `try`: tracking has
already succeeded, so a summary failure only logs. Covered by
`tests/e2e/track-all-summary.mjs`.

**Every tracking pass marks the project dirty.** Track Frame, Track Frame Range
and Track All rewrite `session.instanceGroups`, `session.frameIdentityMap` and
`session.identities`, so each calls `markDirty()` — `trackCurrentFrame` before it
drops the frame's groups, `runTrackingPass` before `clearIdSwitchResults` and its
own clear, so both sweeps (`runCrossViewTrackerProgress` and the windowed
`sweepTrackAllFrames`) are covered by the one call. The call sits
AFTER every bail-out (a refused click is not an edit) and is deliberately NOT
conditional on what the run found: the clear runs first, so a re-run that
matches nothing — or throws partway — has still wiped the previous result. Same
placement rule as `triangulateAllFrames`. Covered by
`tests/e2e/track-marks-dirty.mjs`, the Track All step of
`tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs` and `tests/test-tracker-gui.mjs`.
Those tests switch the automatic ID-switch checks off, because a check that runs
calls `markDirty()` itself and would hide a tracker that never does.

**Animal-count auto-detect is a resident SAMPLE, deliberately.**
`computeMaxInstancesPerView` (used when the user has not set a count) reads
`session.frameGroups`, so on a lazy project it samples the resident window rather
than the project. It is NOT converted to a store sweep like the other
resident-only defects: every animal is visible in most frames, so the max is hit
almost immediately, and changing how the count is derived would change tracking
output on a path that works. The error is one-directional (too LOW, only if no
sampled frame shows every animal at once) and surfaces as a too-small identity
pool, so it logs a warning naming the sample size and pointing at the explicit
setting.

**Node weights.** Both the app's CrossViewTracker and the bench-only
`matchFrameInstances` honor per-node weights from the Tracking Wizard
(`getNodeWeightArray`, `ui/settings.js`). `runCrossViewTracker` passes the
resolved weight array into the tracker (`hp.nodeWeights`), where each node's
contribution to the 2D + 3D association cost is scaled and weight-0 nodes are
dropped. `matchFrameInstances` applies the same weights to `epipolarScore`,
`reprojectionScore`, the 3D-distance signal in `reorderGroupsByPrevTargets`, and
each `computeInstanceDistance`. `null` weights ⇒ every node weighted 1.

**Tracking thresholds.** `matchFrameInstances` snapshots the user-editable
tracking thresholds (`_thresholds = getTrackingThresholds()`, `ui/settings.js`);
`thr(id)` reads that snapshot (falling back to live defaults): `epipolarDecay`,
`reprojSigma`, `epipolarWeight`/`reprojWeight`, `minMatchScore`,
`prevIdentityBonus`, and `reprojectionGate(nViews)` →
`reprojGate2`/`reprojGate3`/`reprojGate4`. Defaults reproduce the prior
constants exactly.

**Benchmark-derived levers (bench-only).** Two thresholds on the legacy
`matchFrameInstances` matcher port the productive levers from the
`G_keeptrack_3d6` benchmark champion; both default to a no-op:
- *`track3dWeight` (default 1).* Weight of Signal 2 (3D-position distance) in
  `reorderGroupsByPrevTargets`' weighted average; ≈6 makes 3D continuity
  dominate (the reference's `correspondence_weight_3d`).
- *`filterMinVisibleNodes`/`filterMinInstanceScore`/`filterMinMeanNodeScore`
  (all default 0 = off).* `collectInstances`, `buildTrackerDetections` and
  `buildTrackerInputs` (the last two given the session, which a lazy project's
  node scores need) gate every instance through `passesDetectionFilter`
  (`pose/detection-filter-stats.js`, which the Tracking Wizard's readout also
  counts with, so the two cannot disagree): fewer than N present keypoints
  (`inst.countPoints()`), or — when a per-instance `score` exists — below the
  score, or a prediction whose mean node score (sleap-nn's, a missing node
  counting as 0) is below `filterMinMeanNodeScore`. Full parity (OKS dedup, gap
  recovery) is not implemented. `runSingleCameraTrackAll` reads the same three
  settings (through `thr`) and hands them to `trackSingleCamera` as
  `opts.filter`: one camera applies the same gate before SLEAP's tracker.

Covered by `tests/test-tracker-luc3d.mjs` (filter + weight on the real
`matchFrameInstances`) and `tests/test-tracker-gui.mjs` (track/identity
assignment + GUI refresh contract); both run under Node via the bench-style
UI-stubbing loaders (`scripts/bench/hooks.mjs`, `tests/tracker-gui-hooks.mjs`).

**Note.** `reorderGroupsByPrevTargets` passes a true `nTargets × nGroups`
rectangular cost matrix to `hungarianAlgorithm` (no pre-padding to square). The
solver's internal padding strips padded-row claims via its `p[j4] <= n` guard,
so padded rows cannot steal real group columns (which surfaced as duplicate
identity colors).

**Duplicate-identity guards in `matchFrameInstances`:**
- *Incremental triangulation.* The "add remaining cameras" stage iterates (up to
  `MAX_REFINE_PASSES`), re-triangulating each group from ALL attached views
  every pass, so a fragile 2-view seed does not push real instances past the gate.
- *Adaptive gate.* `reprojectionGate(nViews)` — tight (100 px) for a 2-view
  seed, looser (140/180) once 3+ views make the estimate trustworthy.
- *Single-view groups get no identity.* Any group with `size < 2` is skipped
  (not geometrically verified) and falls through to the explicit-none guard.
- *Explicit "no identity".* A negative sentinel (`EXPLICIT_NONE`) is written
  per-frame for every visible instance that landed in no group, so
  `getIdentity*ForTrack` returns null instead of falling back to the stale
  global `trackIdentityMap` (the two getters in `pose-data.js` treat a negative
  per-frame value as "none"). `Session.isExplicitNoIdentity(cam, trackIdx,
  frameIdx)` reports that sentinel specifically (distinct from "no entry").
  Consumers: overlays color such instances space gray (`NULL_ID_COLOR`) when
  coloring by identity; the timeline gives them a gray "No ID" row per camera;
  and the identity-grouping passes leave them unlinked — both
  `triangulateCurrentFrame` (`triangulation.js`) and
  `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll` (`ui/export-modals.js`).

**WHICH function the Triangulate button calls.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`
(`wireActionMenu('triangulateDropdown', …)`, its `all` handler — a click on
the button, or its menu's All frames) routes by the default method AND by
session state: `'ba'` →
`triangulateAllFrames('ba')`; default/DLT with **any identities present** →
`groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll('dlt')` (`ui/export-modals.js`); DLT with no
identities → `triangulateAllFrames('dlt')`. Any real tracked project takes the
MIDDLE branch, so `triangulateAllFrames` is NOT the function a user exercises
with DLT. Diagnostics must drive `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll` (see
`tests/e2e/_diag-real-playback-overlays.mjs`).

**It must never delete a frame's groups it cannot rebuild.**
`groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll` deletes a frame's `instanceGroups` and
rebuilds only identity buckets resolving on >= 2 cameras via
`getIdentityIdForTrack(cam, inst.trackIdx, frameIdx)`. On a reopened project that
lookup can return null for every instance (identity is carried on
`group.identityId`, and the per-frame entries need not key by the rehydrated
`trackIdx`), which emptied every frame. So it (a) seeds the buckets from the
existing groups' `identityId` when the per-frame lookup yields nothing, and (b)
returns early WITHOUT touching the frame when nothing would be rebuilt, warning
instead. It also guards `fg === undefined` (the sweep's contract) — otherwise the
first 3D-only frame throws mid-sweep, after deletes have run on an arbitrary
prefix of the project.

**Null-node status.** After a run, `trackCurrentFrame` / `trackAll` count the
null (non-triangulated) 3D nodes across the groups the tracker formed
(`countNullNodesInTargets` over each frame's `targets3d`; single-view groups with
no `points3d` are skipped) and show the total in the status bar
(`#statusNullNodes`, `setNullNodesStatus`) and the completion message — the
headline metric for comparing node-weight settings.

**Auto-cap.** When the animal count is blank (the first-use dialog answered
empty, or the toolbar's Animals field cleared), `trackAll` / `trackCurrentFrame`
resolve `numAnimals` via `computeMaxInstancesPerView(session)` — the largest
instance count seen in any (camera, frame) pair — instead of null; uncapped,
leftover groups each spawn a fresh `addIdentity('id_N')` and the identity pool
drifts upward.

**Key exports.**
- `matchFrameInstances(frameGroup, cameras, session, opts)` — match all
  instances in one frame across views; returns groups + identity
  assignments.
- `trackCurrentFrame()` — toolbar / Edit menu "Track Frame" handler.
- `findMatchForSelected()` — Edit menu "Find Match" (latent bug: it calls
  `globalTracker.findMatchesForInstance`, which `CrossViewTracker` does not
  define, so it throws; the source's TODO about `CrossViewTracker` being
  undefined is stale — it is imported now).
- `trackAll()` — toolbar "Track All" handler (drives `CrossViewTracker`; see
  "Tracker engine" below). `trackFrameRange(startFrame, endFrame)` — Track Frame
  Range. `trackableFrameBounds(session)` — the session's real frame extent.
- `commitTrackedFrame`, `runCrossViewTracker`, `runCrossViewTrackerProgress` —
  see "Tracker engine" below.
- `getTrackerNumAnimals()` / `setTrackerNumAnimals(n)` /
  `onTrackerNumAnimalsChange(fn)` — the ACTIVE session's animal count
  (`Session.numAnimals`, saved with the project; the getter returns `null` for
  auto-detect and for never-asked alike).
  The first-use dialog (`askTrackSetup` → `ui/track-setup-modal.js`) opens only
  while the session has **no answer at all** (`numAnimalsAnswered`: `numAnimals`
  is `null`): a blank answer is stored as `'auto'`, so it is not re-asked on
  every run. Its count is pre-filled with the session's, else the last count
  given in any session this page has seen (`_lastNumAnimals`). It also lists the
  skeleton's **node weights**, editable: they live in the Tracking Wizard and are
  saved per BROWSER, so a 0 left from another project would otherwise silently
  drop a node from matching. On Continue `askTrackSetup` writes the weights
  (MERGED into `getNodeWeights()` through `updateNodeWeights`, so another
  skeleton's overrides survive) and then the count, both before
  `createTrackerRun` snapshots them; Cancel writes nothing and the run bails.
  Track All's copy (`askTrackSetup(..., withImageCheck)`) also offers the image
  ID-switch check — its switch and four options (`IMAGE_CHECK_IDS`, from
  `getTrackingThresholdDefs()`), written through `updateTrackingThresholds`; the
  pass reads `autoImageSwitchCheck` when it ends, so the answer applies to this
  run. `trackCurrentFrame` is `async` for this (its callers ignore the return
  value). Track Frame Range never opens it: its own dialog collects the count.
  Single-camera Track All (`runSingleCameraTrackAll`) asks through the same
  dialog with `noWeights`: the count and the image check, no node weights
  (SLEAP's tracker does not read them).
  `setTrackerNumAnimals` counts as an answer, null included; on a change it
  calls `markDirty()` and notifies the listeners — how the toolbar's Animals
  field (`ui/animal-count-field.js`) follows the prompt and the Track Frame Range
  dialog. With no session it is a no-op. Loading never goes through it — the
  readers set `numAnimals` directly, so a reopened project is not dirty.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./single-camera-tracking.js` — `singleCameraName`, `singleCameraTrackerConfig`,
  `trackSingleCamera`, `SINGLE_CAMERA_LAZY_REASON` (the one-camera Track All).
- `./detection-filter-stats.js` — `passesDetectionFilter` (the detection-pool
  filter; see above).
- `./pose-data.js` — `InstanceGroup`, `points3dNodeCount`, `hasPoint3d`,
  `readPoint3d`, `pooledPoints3d` (`commitTrackedFrame` stores a COPY of the
  target's 3D in the slab pool, so the group does not share the target's live
  array).
- `./triangulation.js` — `computeFundamentalMatrix`, `triangulatePointDLT`,
  `triangulatePoints`, `reprojectPoint`, `reprojectPoints`,
  `computeInstanceDistanceTo`, `hungarianAlgorithm`, `loadAllLazyFrames` /
  `sweepLazyFrameWindows` (the Track All sweeps), and
  `showCalibrationRequiredPopup`.
- `./placeholder-cameras.js` — `hasRealCalibration`: Track This Frame and
  `runTrackingPass` (Track All, Track Frame Range) refuse with the Calibration
  Required popup when every camera is a placeholder (a folder loaded with no
  calibration), as Triangulate does — cross-view tracking on cameras that all
  sit at the origin would build groups from 3D with no baseline.
- `./cross-view-tracker.js` — `CrossViewTracker`, `Detection`.
- `./run-state.js` — `noteTrackRun` (after `trackCurrentFrame`'s and
  `runTrackingPass`'s run): the blue Track button.
- `../ui/app-state.js` — `state`, `interactionManager`, `timeline`,
  `viewport3d`, `getActiveSession`.
- `../ui/settings.js` — `getNodeWeightArray`, `getNodeWeight`,
  `updateNodeWeights`, `getTrackingThresholdDefs`, `updateTrackingThresholds`
  (the first-use dialog), `getTrackingThresholds`, `getTrackingThreshold`,
  `isCameraTracked` (both `trackAll`/`trackCurrentFrame` drop cameras where it is
  false; abort with a warning if fewer than 2 views remain).
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `markDirty`, `setStatus`, `hideLoading`.
- `../ui/loading-overlay.js` — `showLoadingProgress`, `createProgressPacer`,
  `yieldToPaint`, `idleWhile` / `busyDuring` (the waits on the tracker worker
  are idle time, its commits are not), and `setLoadingCpu` as the identity pass
  starts: on the worker `{mainThread: true, cpuBusyMs: pool.cpuBusyMs}` (the
  worker's busy time plus the main thread's minus its waits); inline
  `{mainThread: true}` alone — one core's share. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/track-all-cpu-load.mjs` (both), which also pins the progress
  line's, status line's and summary box's thousands commas.
- `./tracker-pool.js` — `startTrackerWorker` (see "Track All runs its tracker on
  a worker" below).
- `../ui/track-setup-modal.js` — `showTrackSetupModal`, `IMAGE_CHECK_IDS`: the
  first-use dialog (`askTrackSetup`, above). A leaf, so no cycle.
- `../ui/rendering.js` — `drawAllOverlays`, `showPredictedOnly`,
  `PREDICTED_ONLY_NOTE`: Track Frame (when it found targets), Track Frame Range
  and Track All (when they assigned identities) end showing ONLY the Predicted
  layer — User, Reprojections, Errors unticked — and append the note to the
  status line when that changed anything.
- `../ui/info-panel.js` — `updateInfoPanel`.
- `../ui/id-switch-modal.js` — `runIdSwitchChecks`, `clearIdSwitchResults`: after
  a successful Track All or Track Frame Range that assigned 2+ identities,
  `runTrackingPass` runs the ID-switch checks over the whole session (`{auto:
  true, statusPrefix, size, image}`: size per `autoSwitchCheck` (default on),
  images per `autoImageSwitchCheck` (default off)) and awaits them, so the pass
  resolves after the checks. It drops the session's earlier results and markers
  (`clearIdSwitchResults(session)`) before clearing identities, for both paths.
  Its return value (each result carries `elapsedMs`) feeds the Track All summary.
- `../ui/track-summary.js` — `summarizeTrackedIdentities`, `describeSwitchCheck`
  (the Track All summary's data).
- `../ui/track-summary-modal.js` — `showTrackSummaryModal` (with the session, so
  it shows the Identities section, `ui/identity-gallery.js`);
  `rememberTrackSummary`: keeps it for reopening.
- `../ui/timeline-controller.js` — `collapseTimeline`: a successful Track All
  (not a range) closes the Timeline if it is open.
- `../ui/panel-visibility.js` — `collapseViewport3D`: the same, for the 3D viewer
  (passed `viewport3d` from `../ui/app-state.js`, which is also imported).
  No cycle: that module imports app-state, save-load, loading-overlay, settings,
  `pose/id-switch-check.js` and `ui/image-embedder.js`, none of which import the
  tracker.
- `../ui/color-by.js` — `setColorByIdentity`: a successful Track All / Track
  Frame Range that assigned identities switches Color from Tracks to ID and says
  so in the status line. Track Frame (one frame) does not.
- `../ui/trail-presets.js` — `setTrailSeconds`, `trailSecondsName`,
  `TRACK_ALL_TRAIL_SECONDS`: a successful Track All that assigned identities
  turns node trails on at ¼ s **if they are off** (each animal's recent path is
  where an identity switch shows) and says so in the status line. A length the
  user already picked is kept; Track Frame and Track Frame Range leave trails
  alone; single-camera Track All turns them on too. Through the shared setter, so ui-wiring's handler moves the menus'
  checkmarks and the button's blue. Covered by `tests/test-tracker-gui.mjs`,
  `tests/e2e/track-all-turns-on-trails.mjs` and `tests/e2e/single-camera-track-all.mjs` §1.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/track-range-modal.js`,
`ui/animal-count-field.js`.

**User-facing features.** "Track Frame" / "Track All" buttons, identity
propagation across frames, find-match-for-selected.

**Track All runs its tracker on a worker** (`pose/tracker-pool.js` +
`pose/tracker-worker.js`). `runTrackingPass` makes the run
(`createTrackerRun`, which keeps `hp` on it) and `attachTrackerWorker`
starts ONE worker with it; the tracker is sequential, so the parallelism is the
main thread hydrating frames, building inputs and committing results WHILE the
worker tracks. Per frame, `submitTrackerFrame` builds `buildTrackerInputs`
(`buildTrackerDetections`' filter and slot order exactly; per camera, each
passing detection's `_xy` plus a kept `{frameIdx, instance, unlinkedId}` per
slot) and submits it; `commitWorkerFrame` turns the worker's targets back into
the shape `commitTrackedFrame` reads off a `CrossViewTracker` (targets in order,
each one's this-frame members in its `detsByCam` order, camera indices back to
names) and calls it — the commit is the inline one, unchanged, so identities,
`frameIdentityMap`, groups, promotions and 3D are bit-identical to the inline run
(`tests/e2e/track-all-worker.mjs`). Both loops take the run:
`trackFrameListProgress` (the non-windowed one; `runCrossViewTrackerProgress` is
a wrapper over it, inline) and `sweepTrackAllFrames(session, run, onProgress,
opts)`, which on the worker sweeps with `releaseLag: 1` and `beforeRelease →
committedThrough(end - 1)`: a window stays resident until its results are
written into its FrameGroups, while the next one hydrates. Progress reports the
frames COMMITTED, not sent. If the worker cannot start, both run inline;
`window.LUCID_TRACKER_WORKER = 0` forces that. The gain is what the main thread
had besides tracking — a lazy project's hydration (~30% faster on a 108,000-frame
lazy project) — so an eager project gains little. A/B and profiling tools:
`tests/e2e/_diag-trackall-ab.mjs`, `tests/e2e/_diag-trackall-worker-profile.mjs`.

**Tracker engine.** `trackCurrentFrame` / `trackAll` drive the
`CrossViewTracker` (`pose/cross-view-tracker.js`) exclusively — the app's only
tracker. `runCrossViewTracker(session, cameras, frameIndices, propagate,
maxTargets)` is a synchronous loop (single-frame tracking + the bench/test
harnesses, which read its return value). Its per-frame body is factored into
`createTrackerRun`/`stepTrackerFrame`, reused by the async
`runCrossViewTrackerProgress(…, onProgress)`: identical association, yielding on
a ~250 ms clock (`createProgressPacer`) and awaiting `onProgress(done, total)`
before each yield and once at the end, so Track All shows a live progress line
and determinate bar. A non-windowed lazy session runs a "Loading frames" stage
first (Step 1 of 2) through `loadAllLazyFrames`' `(msg, done, total)` callback;
the windowed path hands `onProgress` to `sweepLazyFrameWindows`.
`buildTrackerDetections` wraps each linked/unlinked instance as a `Detection`;
`commitTrackedFrame` persists, per frame, one `InstanceGroup` per live target
(with `identityId` + `points3d`), maps each target's stable trackId to a session
`Identity`, writes `setFrameIdentity`, and promotes unlinked members into the
linked pool.

**Raw-trackIdx collision guard.** Per-camera prediction files number tracks
independently, and a camera's raw tracker can briefly give the SAME trackIdx to
two animals in one camera on one frame; both `setFrameIdentity` calls would hit
the same `frameIdentityMap` key, and the 2D color path (`getIdentityForTrack`)
would disagree with `group.identityId` (info panel, 3D). `commitTrackedFrame`
tracks every `camName:trackIdx` key it writes in a frame (`writtenThisFrame`); a
second, DIFFERENT identity on the same key marks it explicit "no identity" (-1),
so the 2D lookup falls through to `group.identityId`. Exported so the guard is
unit-testable: `tests/test-tracker-collision-guard.mjs`.

**Identities only, no propagation.** Both Track All and Track Frame pass
`propagate:false`: the tracker writes the per-frame identity map +
InstanceGroups and does NOT rewrite `Instance.trackIdx` — propagation is a
user-chosen step (**Tracks ▸ Propagate IDs → Tracks**,
`propagateIdentitiesToTracks`, or **Tracks → IDs**), so a run never clobbers the
imported track structure. Both filter out views excluded in the Tracking Wizard
(`isCameraTracked`) and abort if fewer than 2 views remain.

**Lazy sessions.** `session.frameIndices` returns only the resident window on a
lazy `.slp`. With a windowing-capable loader (`SioLazyLoader` — `loader.isSync &&
releaseWindow`), Track All drives `sweepTrackAllFrames` (memory-bounded):
materialize a window (`batchLoadLazyFrames`), step the tracker over it
(`stepTrackerFrame` reads only the CURRENT frame's `FrameGroup` — all cross-frame
state lives in the run object), release the window — never a full-project
`loadAllLazyFrames()`. A worker-backed lazy loader (analysis `.h5`, no
windowing) or a non-lazy session still `await loadAllLazyFrames()` and re-read
the full frame list — otherwise it would track only the visited frames.
**Fresh-open guard:** the upfront "any frames?" check uses `loader.nFrames > 0`
when a windowed loader is present, not `frameIndices.length` (0 on a freshly
opened lazy project). Regression test:
`tests/e2e/track-all-fresh-lazy-session.mjs`.

**Hyperparameters** come from the
`corr2dWeight`/`corr3dWeight`/`velocityThreshold`/`distanceThreshold`/`timePenalty`/`stale`/`matchGate`
tracking thresholds (`ui/settings.js`, via `crossViewHyperparams()`; defaults are
the `G_keeptrack_3d6` champion values, except `distanceThreshold`/`stale` — see
`pose/cross-view-tracker.js`). Track Frame/Track All pass the user's animal count
as `maxTargets` (a LUCID divergence; `null` = uncapped). Covered by
`tests/test-crossview-populate.mjs` (data-structure population),
`tests/test-cross-view-tracker.mjs` (algorithm), and
`tests/test-tracker-collision-guard.mjs`.

**Track Frame Range.** `trackFrameRange(startFrame, endFrame)` tracks a
contiguous span, so a user can re-run tracking around a known ID switch while
changing Tracking Wizard settings without a full pass. Reached from the Track
Frame split button's hover dropdown via `ui/track-range-modal.js`; **the toolbar
button itself still tracks the current frame**.

`trackAll` and `trackFrameRange` are both one-liners over a shared
`runTrackingPass(range)` (`range === null` ⇒ Track All). Three differences,
all driven by that argument:

- **Which frames are swept.** Windowed lazy sessions pass `start`/`end` through
  `sweepTrackAllFrames` into `sweepLazyFrameWindows`; non-lazy sessions filter
  `frameIndices`. The frame COUNT reported is the range's, not the project's.
- **What prior state is cleared.** Track All wipes `identities` /
  `frameIdentityMap` / `instanceGroups` wholesale. A range clears only entries
  whose frame lies inside `[lo, hi]` (`clearTrackingStateInRange`, which decodes
  `frameIdentityMap` keys through `Session.frameIdentityEntries()` rather than
  re-deriving the packed key layout). Getting this wrong is invisible damage —
  the range on screen still looks right while everything else is destroyed.
- **Whether identities are recycled.** A range run starts the tracker from
  scratch at `lo`, so its targets are fresh every run; without recycling, each
  re-run would mint a new `id_N` per animal and the identity list would grow
  without bound. `runTrackingPass` hands `commitTrackedFrame` an `identityPool`
  of the session's existing identity ids, consumed in order before `addIdentity`
  mints anything. Track All passes no pool (it cleared the list first).

Every frame number in these status strings is **1-based** (`displayFrame()`);
stored indices stay 0-based, and the `console.log` deliberately prints raw
indices (marked `0-based`). Range endpoints are clamped to the session's real
extent (`trackableFrameBounds`, which prefers `lazyLoader.nFrames` over the
resident-only `frameIndices`) and normalized if reversed; a non-integer endpoint
is refused outright (a `NaN` would pass the ordering check and sweep nothing).
A range run does NOT propagate to tracks. `runTrackingPass` skips the first-use
dialog (`askTrackSetup`) on the range path so it never stacks on the range
dialog, which collects the count itself via `setTrackerNumAnimals`.
`runTrackingPass` **returns** `{ok, start, end, identities, frames}` — `ok:false`
for every bail-out, and on success the CLAMPED, normalized span actually swept,
which callers cannot recompute; the modal uses `end` to park the viewer on the
last frame tracked. Covered by `tests/e2e/track-frame-range.mjs`.

**Legacy `matchFrameInstances` (bench-only).** The original per-frame matcher +
4-signal reorder is retained and exported but **not used by the app** — only by
the benchmark harness (`scripts/bench/speed_test.mjs`, `bench_driver.mjs`) and
`tests/test-tracker-luc3d.mjs`. Its thresholds (`epipolarDecay`, `reprojSigma`,
`reprojWeight`, `minMatchScore`, `prevIdentityBonus`, `reprojGate*`,
`track3dWeight`) are hidden from the Tracking Wizard.

---

### pose/cross-view-tracker.js

**Purpose.** `CrossViewTracker` — LUCID's cross-view 3D tracker and the app's
only temporal tracker. Adapted from the `CrossViewTracker` written by Liezl Maree
in the talmolab/sleap-3d repo (Python, `sleap_3d/tracker.py`) and reimplemented
in JS; no longer byte-faithful (stale-anchor fix and match gate, below). It
associates per-camera 2D detections to a running list of 3D `Target`s, one
camera-view at a time, via Hungarian assignment on a cost that sums a 2D
reprojection term and a 3D point-to-ray term. No Kalman filter, no velocity
model (matches the reference). The file header documents the coordinate
conventions, both divergences (with their measurements) and the preserved
quirks; the summary below keeps only the rules.

**Coordinate invariance.** Every cost term is geometric given `target.points3d`,
and every geometry helper it imports is invariant to a rigid change of world
frame; `points3d` (built by `_retriangulate` with DLT) is the only
frame-dependent input, which is why `triangulatePointDLT` had to be made
frame-invariant (see `pose/triangulation-core.js`). Keep it that way: anything
added to the cost must be a function of the rays, the pixels or `points3d`,
never of absolute world coordinates. The tracker calls `triangulatePoints` (DLT)
unconditionally and never `triangulatePointsBA`, so the Settings "Refined"
method does **not** reach this path.

**Stale-anchor fix (divergence from the reference).** Both additive
(zero config reproduces the reference):
- **`stale` (hp, frames, Tracking Wizard default 20).** At the start of every
  `trackFrame()` (`_beginFrame`), evict any `detsByCam` entry older than `stale`
  frames, so `_retriangulate` cannot fuse one fresh view with several ancient
  ones (a frozen ghost after an occlusion permanently swaps identities). `0` =
  unbounded staleness (reference behavior).
- **Frame-synchronous association (unconditional, no flag).** A target's
  `points3d`/`frameIdxMean()` snapshot (`_snapMean`) is frozen at frame start and
  every camera's Hungarian this frame is scored against it; the one
  `_retriangulate()` happens in `_endFrame()`. `_adjacency2d`/`_adjacency3d`
  still read `target.points3d` directly, so calling them outside a
  `trackFrame()` lifecycle (as `tests/test-crossview-features.mjs` does) is
  unaffected (`_snapMean` is `null` there and the live `frameIdxMean()` is
  used). Births (`_initializeTargets`) still retriangulate immediately.
`distanceThreshold`'s Tracking Wizard default is 25 to match;
`scripts/bench/hooks.mjs`'s `THRESHOLD_DEFAULTS` must stay in sync with
`ui/settings.js` (its own comment requires it).

**Match gate (`matchGate` hp; default OFF, 1 = on, 0 = the pre-gate tracker
exactly).** Off by default because on proofread benchmarks it helped SLAP-2M but
gave ~6x the ID switches on Mouse-Dyad-10M and ~10x on s-DANNCE (#285). The
reference Hungarian is forced — every target takes a detection whenever a view
has enough — so a spare target plus a reflection can trade a correct match away.
With the gate on, `_trackView` runs two `_assign` stages: **tracked** targets
(still holding a detection after stale eviction) get one "no match" column each
at adjacency 0, so they only take a detection they score positively on; then
**lost** targets (`_lost`, set in `_beginFrame` when eviction emptied
`detsByCam`) take the leftovers, forced — the re-acquisition path; gating them
too would orphan a reappearing animal. Covered by
`tests/test-cross-view-tracker.mjs` (the swap in miniature, and lost-target
re-acquisition).

**Misplaced nodes (2026-10-20, `maxPenalty` / `matchReproj` / `birthMedian` hp; all
0 = off = today's tracker exactly).** Ported from campy's live port of this tracker
(olinesn/campy#85). A node the 2D model put in the wrong place triangulates far from
its animal (metres, on a rig whose model was trained elsewhere), and the unbounded
per-node 3D term then lets that one node decide the Hungarian. `maxPenalty` floors
the per-node 3D term at `-maxPenalty × corr3d` (4 = flat beyond 5 × distanceThreshold).
`matchReproj` (px) makes `_retriangulate` also build `target.match3d` — `points3d`
re-solved by `checkedPoints3d` (campy's `TriangulateChecked`: a node whose worst view
is over the threshold loses the view WITHOUT which its other views fit best, down to
2 views, then is dropped) — and `matchPoints3d(target)` is what `_adjacency3d` and
`targetProjection` read. `points3d` (written to the groups) stays the plain DLT.
`birthMedian` pairs births by the median epipolar distance over nodes
(`epipolarMedianMatrix`) instead of `epipolarErrorMatrix`'s mean. Measured with an
exact offline replay of Track All on 22 proofread SLAP sessions (8 cameras):
maxPenalty 4 + matchReproj 20 took lasting identity switches 360 → 168 and mean
accuracy 88.2% → 90.5% (every pair of 1–8 × 10–40 px halved the switches), but on
the 5-mouse recording no combination beat the default (4 → 4–14 lasting relabels).
With 3 of the cameras at a time (3 triples × 22 sessions) switches went 1,598 → 704
and mean accuracy 82.8% → 89.8%. birthMedian changed nothing, with 8 cameras or 3. Hence opt-in: the first two as
Tracking Wizard settings; `birthMedian` only as a hyperparameter (no measured effect,
so not offered). `matchReproj`'s cost is the leave-one-view-out re-solves
(SLAP 145420: 1.0M node checks, 19% over 20 px, 1.6M trial solves). `checkedPoints3d`
does them through `DltViewSystem`, reads the extrinsics from one flat array, and tries
both the trials and each trial's views WORST VIEW FIRST, so a losing trial stops at
its first view (ties still go to the lowest view index, via `nextUp`). Bit-identical
to solving each trial with `triangulatePointDLT` in index order (identical identities
on 22 SLAP sessions + 5 mice); what `matchReproj` adds fell 43–49% (maxPenalty 4 +
matchReproj 20: 5.1 → 4.0 s on 145420, 51 → 39 s on 5 mice). Covered by
`tests/test-crossview-features.mjs` (each option's effect, that 0 reproduces today's
cost, and `match3d` against a verbatim copy of the original per-trial loop).

**Coordinate conventions.** Works entirely in NORMALIZED camera coordinates
(detections undistorted + K⁻¹-applied on ingest, `normalizePoint`); the
"projection matrix" is the bare extrinsic `[R|t]` (`camera.extrinsicMatrix`).
`distanceThreshold` is in world units (mm); `velocityThreshold` in normalized
image units (so the 3D term dominates and `corr3dWeight` is the meaningful knob).

**Score caches.** `_assign` scores every target against every detection of a
view; two inputs depend on only one side of the pair and are cached:
- **`detectionRays(det, ext)`** — the detection back-projected to one ray per
  node, cached on the `Detection` (`_rays`, `_raysExt`).
- **`targetProjection(target, ext)`** — the target's 3D projected into a camera,
  cached on the `Target` (`_proj = {p3, ext, pts}`), so once per (target, view).
Both key on OBJECT IDENTITY, which is exactly when their inputs change:
`target.points3d` is only ever REPLACED (`_retriangulate`; nothing edits it in
place), and `Camera.setExtrinsics` replaces `extrinsicMatrix`. Keep both true.
The per-view projection is reusable only because of the frame-synchronous rule
above. Pinned by `tests/test-cross-view-tracker.mjs` "Score caches".

**Exports.** `CrossViewTracker` (class: `trackFrame(detsByCam, camsOrder)`,
maintains `.targets`), `Detection` (2D observation: `pointsNorm`/`pointsPixel` +
`cam`/`frameIdx`/`slot`), `normalizePoint`.

**Faithful-port quirks still preserved (do NOT "fix" without new measurement).**
`velocity`/`distance` thresholds are SOFT (drive the cost negative, not hard
gates) and, with the gate off, negative matches are not filtered; the 3D term
ignores the time gap; 3D velocity is zero; re-triangulation is plain DLT over all
(freshness-filtered) stored per-view detections. Adds a defensive `nansum`-style
skip of non-finite cost terms (robust to a degenerate `[I|0]` camera).

**LUCID divergence — `maxTargets` (opt-in target cap).** The reference has NO
animal-count cap. When `hp.maxTargets` is a positive integer,
`_initializeTargets` stops spawning births once that many live targets exist
(leftover detections are dropped for the frame and re-acquired by matching next
frame). `null`/omitted restores reference behavior. Wired from Track All / Track
Frame via the user's animal count.

**LUCID divergence — `nodeWeights` (per-node association weights).** Optional
`hp.nodeWeights` array (indexed to `Instance.points`); `_adjacency2d`/
`_adjacency3d` scale each node's cost by `_nodeWeight(k)` and skip weight-0
nodes entirely. `null`/omitted ⇒ every node weighted 1. Wired from
`runCrossViewTracker` via the Tracking Wizard's Node Weights section
(`getNodeWeightArray`).

**Imports.** `pose/triangulation-core.js` (`triangulatePoints`, `DltViewSystem`,
`reprojectPoint`, `backProjectToRays`, `pointsToRayDistances`, `hungarianAlgorithm`,
`computeFundamentalMatrix`, `epipolarErrorMatrix`); `pose/pose-data.js`
(`points3dNodeCount`, `readPoint3d`). From the core, NOT `pose/triangulation.js`
(which re-exports them): that module pulls in the whole UI, and this one must
load in `pose/tracker-worker.js`. Keep every import of this module worker-safe.

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js`, `pose/tracker-worker.js`.

---

### pose/sleap-tracker.js

**Purpose.** SLEAP's single-camera pose tracker: a port of `sleap_nn.tracking`
(talmolab/sleap-nn @ 3d21684419ca, 2026-10-02 — `Tracker`, `run_tracker`,
`connect_single_breaks`, the `fixed_window` and `local_queues` candidates,
`utils.py`) and of `evaluation.compute_oks`. It is what `sleap-nn track` runs on
an existing predictions file. Used by single-camera Track All
(`pose/single-camera-tracking.js`). Pure — no DOM, no app state, no imports.

**It agrees with upstream on every track id**, ties included, and that is the
point of the module: a SLEAP user re-tracking in LUCID gets the answer
`sleap-nn track` would give. Measured: 9 full proofread SLAP videos (both
candidate methods) and 9 option combinations, ~520,000 detections, all
identical; ~0.35–0.65 s per 18,000-frame video in JS against ~50 s in Python
(most of which is sleap-nn decoding every video frame it never reads). Ties are
common — OKS at the default stddev underflows to exactly 0 for poses more than
~1.5 body lengths apart — so everything that decides one is ported exactly:
- `linearSumAssignment` — SciPy's `rectangular_lsap.cpp` (Crouse's shortest
  augmenting path), line for line, including its reverse `remaining` order and
  the transpose of a tall matrix.
- `numpyArgsort` — `np.argsort`'s generic introsort (median-of-3 partitions,
  `SMALL_QUICKSORT = 15`, insertion sort below), which greedy matching and NMS
  read their ties from. It is NOT stable. This is what numpy runs on arm64 and
  on x86 without AVX-512; with AVX-512 numpy dispatches to x86-simd-sort, so
  upstream itself breaks those ties differently there. Checked on 406
  tie-heavy arrays.
- `pySetOrder` — CPython 3.11's set iteration order (open addressing, 9 linear
  probes, ×4 resize at 3/5 full). `fixed_window`'s `current_tracks` is
  `list(set(...))`, so this IS its score-matrix column order.
- OKS sums the per-node similarities in numpy's pairwise order (`pairwiseSum`;
  plain pairwise over all elements, confirmed bit-exact on 20,000 sums).
What is not bit-exact: `Math.exp` vs numpy's `exp` (last ulp) and
`euclidean_dist` on keypoints (BLAS `dot` in numpy). Both only matter in a
near-tie; none showed up on the real data.

**Not ported:** `FlowShiftTracker` (optical flow on the video),
`KalmanShiftTracker` (EM-fitted Kalman filter — on 6 proofread cameras sleap-nn's
own Kalman tracker made 39 lasting swap events to the base tracker's 16), the
appearance blend and the `embeddings`/`masks` features.

**Key exports.** `SLEAP_TRACKER_DEFAULTS` (sleap-nn's `run_tracker` defaults, in
camelCase: `windowSize` 5, `candidatesMethod` 'fixed_window', `features`
'keypoints', `scoringMethod` 'oks', `scoringReduction` 'mean', `oksStddev`
null -> 0.025, `trackMatchingMethod` 'hungarian', `maxTracks`,
`postConnectSingleBreaks`, `targetInstanceCount`, pre-/clean-cull options);
`SleapTracker` (class: `new SleapTracker(cfg, observe?)` — `observe` is called
after each frame's matching with `{frameIdx, dets, tracks, cost, nr, nc, pairs}`
and is read-only, so parity is untouched; `track(dets, frameIdx)` -> per detection a track id,
`null` = dropped, `undefined` = a prediction left alone on a frame with user
instances — sleap-nn tracks only those there); `runSleapTracker(frames, cfg)` /
`runSleapTrackerAsync(frames, cfg, {onProgress, signal, yieldEvery})` (the
whole `run_tracker`, post-processing included); `connectSingleBreaks`;
`instancesOverCount` (culling); `computeOks`; `countValidPoints`;
`linearSumAssignment`; `numpyArgsort`; `pySetOrder`. A detection is
`{points: Float64Array(2K) NaN-missing, score, isUser?}`.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `pose/single-camera-tracking.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-sleap-tracker.mjs` against
`tests/fixtures/sleap-tracker/reference.json` — 900 frames of synthetic
detections (close passes, missed detections, missing nodes, spurious poses,
empty frames) tracked by upstream sleap-nn under six configs, every track id
compared — plus SciPy's / numpy's / CPython's answers on tie cases. Regenerate
the fixture with `tests/fixtures/sleap-tracker/make_fixture.py <sleap-nn
checkout>` (run with sleap-nn's own Python) when moving the port to a newer
sleap-nn.

---

### pose/single-camera-tracking.js

**Purpose.** Track All and the ID-switch checks on a session with ONE camera — a
plain SLEAP predictions file opened with File ▸ Load SLP, which gets one
placeholder `Camera` and every detection unlinked. With one view there is no
cross-view matching and no 3D, so Track All runs SLEAP's tracker
(`pose/sleap-tracker.js`) instead, in sleap-nn's recommended known-count setup:
`local_queues`, `max_tracks` = `tracking_target_instance_count` = the animal
count, connect single breaks.

**It writes the TRACKS**, as `sleap-nn track` does — unlike multi-camera Track
All, which writes identities only and leaves Propagate IDs → Tracks to the
user. On one camera a track IS an animal's identity, and tracks are what a
saved `.slp` hands back to SLEAP. `session.tracks` becomes `track_0…`, every
instance's `trackIdx` is rewritten, and identity `id_k` (id k) is tied to track
k through `frameIdentityMap` — keyed by track, so identity follows the track
from then on. A detection the tracker drops (over the animal count, too few
points) becomes trackless, as do predictions on a frame that has user instances.
Occluded nodes are fed to the tracker as missing: SLEAP's invisible points read
as NaN in sleap-nn.

**The default OKS tolerance is 0.1, not sleap-nn's 0.025** (Tracking Wizard
`scOksStddev`). On 35 proofread 10-min SLAP camera videos (3–4 mice, different
coat colours; each track paired 1:1 with a proofread animal): sleap-nn's
known-count defaults 85.8% correct, 156 lasting swaps; at 0.1, 93.6% and 102;
0.15 93.1%; 0.2 and above worse again (0.5: 75.1%). The mechanism is general —
at 0.025 every pose more than ~1.5 body lengths from a track scores exactly 0,
so after a fast move or a gap the assignment is decided by tie-breaking.
Window 10 was about the same as 5 (88.4% at 0.025), 30 much worse.

**Duplicate removal, opt-in** (Tracking Wizard `scRemoveDuplicates`, default
off; `scDuplicateIou` 0.5, `scDuplicateOks` 0.6 → `trackSingleCamera`'s
`opts.duplicates`). SLEAP sometimes puts a second instance on an animal it
already detected, often partial and scoring 0.4–0.9. With the animal count as
the track cap that copy takes a track, and a lone animal can hop onto it. On a
SLEAP user's proofread two-mouse dataset (6 top-camera sessions, 40 fps) the subject, alone before its partner entered, hopped tracks at
its own duplicates in 2 of 6 sessions, and the partner then entered on its old
track (96.95% correct). sleap-nn's pre-cull cannot help: it acts only on a
frame with MORE detections than the animal count. `duplicateDetections(dets, o)`
leaves out, per frame and before the tracker (the port is untouched), every
detection that duplicates a stronger one (higher score; a user instance is never
removed and is kept first): box IoU (`boxIou`, +1 px as sleap-nn counts it) at
least `iou` AND OKS at least `oks`. Box overlap alone would also take two
touching animals, and keypoints alone two look-alike poses side by side. The OKS
(`duplicateOks`) reads the WEAKER detection's nodes, since a copy is often
partial, against the stronger one, scaled by the stronger one's box (sleap-nn's
`compute_oks` cocoeval normalisation, stddev 0.1; fewer than 3 nodes is never a
duplicate). Removed detections keep no track or identity and stay in the
session; Track All reports how many (`duplicates`, kept apart from `untracked`).
Measured 2026-10-12, offline through this function and then in the app:
- That dataset: every IoU 0.3–0.7 × OKS 0.5–0.8 removes both hops (99.98%,
  no swaps). At the defaults it removes 91 detections, none a real animal.
- 87 proofread SLAP top/mid views (2–4 mice, 3.78M detections): about neutral,
  88.52% -> 88.73% correct and swaps 276 -> 257, with 8 views better and 8
