  worse. It removes 1,231 detections, 74 of them a real second animal.
- The swings are the tracker re-routing when junk is removed, not mistakes of
  the rule: removing only the detections the proofreader also deleted swings
  the same way. Hence off by default.
- Rules tried and not adopted: true IoU is stricter than sleap-nn's own
  "overlap / the weaker box" (used by `pre_cull_iou_threshold`), but scored
  better on SLAP at the same OKS. A keypoint-distance-only rule (median < 10 px)
  removed more, including more real animals: 88.03%, 16 views worse.

**The Tracking Wizard's detection filters apply here too** (`opts.filter`:
`{minVisibleNodes, minInstanceScore, minMeanNodeScore}`, which Track All reads
from `filterMinVisibleNodes` / `filterMinInstanceScore` /
`filterMinMeanNodeScore` exactly as the multi-camera pass does). Per frame and
FIRST — before duplicate removal, which then looks for duplicates among the
detections the filters kept, and before the port, which is untouched — every
detection failing `passesDetectionFilter` (`pose/detection-filter-stats.js`, the
multi-camera tracker's own rule) is left out. So the Wizard's readout counts
exactly what is left out here. Like a duplicate, a filtered detection keeps no
track or identity, stays in the session, and is counted (`filtered`, apart from
`untracked` and `duplicates`). A frame left with no detection is skipped, as a
frame that never had one is; `frames` still counts it (every frame with a
detection before anything was left out). All three minimums default to 0 (off),
which leaves the tracking as it was. Keep them off by default unless measured: on
the offline harness (2026-10-27, `filter_strays.mjs`) dropping detections with
fewer than 2/3/5 visible nodes before Track All helped Track All slightly but
was worse after review — it drops real, partly visible animals (SLAP held-out
after review 99.75% -> 99.05% at >= 3 nodes; 0.4–2% of real detections lost).

**The summary box's gallery reads the same stand-in with a pixel camera**
(`singleCameraCheckSession(session, {pixelCamera: true})`): its one camera's
`project` maps (x, y, 0) back to (x, y) and claims no size, which is what
`ui/identity-gallery.js` needs to cut samples and draw paths.

**The ID-switch checks read a stand-in** (`singleCameraCheckSession`): one
InstanceGroup-shaped object per identified detection, its 2D as `points3d`
(x, y, 0), the detection under `instances` (what the image check crops). The
checks are relative to the median body size and standardise their features, so
pixels instead of millimetres cost nothing; but a 2D bone length also changes
with posture and distance from the camera, so body size is a weaker cue on one
camera. It is OPTIONAL there: single-camera Track All does not run it (the
user's choice, 2026-10-12) and the menu runs it.
- **Measured offline 2026-10-12** (`checkSizeSwitches` on the stand-in after the
  same Track All, its -1 default; label-free since #310):
  - **87 proofread SLAP top/mid views:** per-contact AUC 0.912, 32 rows (22
    real, 2 naming the wrong pair, 8 false), 27 of 276 swaps found. Fixing the
    rows that help on their own: 88.52% -> 90.23%; fixing every row: 87.58%.
  - **The six sessions' back views (12):** AUC 0.984, 3 rows, none false.
  - **The 5-mouse topC:** 4 rows, all false, and both swaps missed (four
    similar-sized mice).
  - **A user's two-mouse dataset:** 1 row, a real error.
  - **Thresholds:** -1.5 halves the false rows but finds 17 swaps instead of 27.
- **History:** the classifier it replaced was why the check was first left off
  on one camera. On 35 SLAP videos (59 real swaps after Track All) it caught 2
  at -50 (encounter AUC 0.55, 6 false rows); the image check —
with talmolab/luc3d#286's episode scoring, which was unmerged at the time —
caught 19 at its -200 default for 15 false rows (0.4 per 10 min), 32 s per
10-min, 3-mouse video. Most of the swaps it misses are not at an encounter it
scores (multi-animal contacts, or one animal's detection missing for seconds
while its track takes another animal).

**A fix swaps the tracks** (`swapSingleCameraIdentities`), so the saved file
carries it, when each of the two identities is one track over the range (always
true after single-camera Track All); otherwise — identities hand-edited apart
from their tracks — it swaps the identity layer alone, like a multi-camera fix.
It is its own inverse, which is how Undo works.

**Where to look: candidate moments.** Most of the switches the checks miss are
not at an encounter they score: they happen in contacts of three animals, or
while one animal's detection is missing for seconds and its track takes another
animal. So Track All also returns `moments` (`candidateMoments`), read from the
tracking itself at no cost: 'ambiguity' — two tracks whose EXCHANGED assignment
would have cost the tracker less than `ambiguityMargin` (0.1, OKS units) more
than the chosen one (through `SleapTracker`'s read-only observer); and
'tracklet' — a tracklet of the INPUT file (SLEAP's own tracking, captured before
it is rewritten) that passes from one identity to another within 5 frames. Per
pair, candidates within 3 s are one moment at the run's last frame. They are
kept on the session as `session._idSwitchCandidates` (NOT saved: the input
tracklets they come from are gone once the project is saved) and tested by the
checks (`pose/id-switch-check.js` `opts.moments`). On the 35 proofread videos
they put a moment within 3 s of 44 of 59 switches (ambiguity alone 17,
tracklets alone 42), about 10 moments per 10 min. Ambiguity is observed before
connect-single-breaks renumbers tracks, so its ids are read through the final
output, never from the observer.

**The review list: where to look, as rows of their own.** Track All also returns
`review` — the candidate moments plus HANDOVERS, clustered the same way
(`candidateMoments(..., extra)` with `handoverCandidates(frames, out, fps)`) — and
`pose/tracker.js` puts it in the ID Switches tab as unscored rows
(`reviewResult(moments, session, fps)` -> a result with `cue: 'review'`, one
'onset' row per moment, `score` 0, `look` = its cues, no far edge). A handover is
a LONE detection (none other within one body length) whose nearest detection in
the last `lookSeconds` (1) lay within `nearBody` (0.5) body lengths and wore
another track (`HANDOVER_DEFAULTS`; body length = median detection extent): the
lone animal hopping onto a stray detection's track, or onto a track another animal
left — the switches no contact sees. 1 s rather than 0.5 because a mouse often
drops out of detection for over half a second and comes back on the other track
(sham_gdx R289: 0.6 s, a switch the 0.5 s list missed entirely); 2 s added places
without catching more. The checks test `moments`: the candidate moments plus
RETURNS, not the handovers (see below).
Measured 2026-10-21/27 offline through this code (the harness rebuilt in
`~/luc3d-eval`, `measure_review.mjs` / `measure_fix.mjs`), a switch counting as on
the list when one of its ends is within 2 s of a row:
- **All of SLAP-2M (2026-10-28), the 195 multi-animal back/mid/top views whose raw
  SLEAP tracks are not already the proofreading (1,632 min, 777 switches):** 1.52 rows
  a minute, 96% of the switches on the list, 15.3% of the video within 2 s of a row;
  2 mice 1.57 / 97% / 16.8%, 3–4 mice 1.37 / 91% / 10.9%; back pair 97%, mid 95%, top
  94%. The 57 views whose raw tracks equal the proofreading: 0.12 a minute, 20 of 20.
  (The 2022-10-19 recordings are 60 fps, the other dates 30; measured at 30 throughout
  these were 1.45 / 95% of 817 / 13.9%. `~/luc3d-eval/lib.mjs` `slapFps`.)
- **Mouse-Dyad-10M (2026-10-28; 2 mice, 150 fps; 244 of its 250 views, six left out
  because their proofread file has 4–12 tracks):** its files hold only the proofread
  detections (no SLEAP tracks, no videos), so Track All ran on them untracked: 90.24%
  right, 172 switches. 2.09 rows a minute, 79% of the switches on the list, 16.1% of
  the video; 84% with sleap-nn's default tracker standing in for the input's tracks. The
  simulated reviewer takes every view to >= 99.93% (Spock `code/dyad-eval/`).
  Those 57 had flattered the earlier 87-view figures below (25 of them were in it):
  there the input-tracklet cue is the answer.
- **87 proofread SLAP top/mid views:** 2 mice (held-out 16) 1.06 rows a minute,
  97% of 232 switches on the list, 10.2% of the video within 2 s of a row; 3–4
  mice (original six) 1.04 a minute, 86% of 44, 7.9%. (With the earlier 0.5 s
  look-back: 0.97 a minute and 96% on 2 mice, 1.02 and 86% on 3–4.)
- **207 sham_gdx conditions (2 mice, mostly a lone subject before its partner
  enters):** 1.13 rows a minute, 94% of 139, 9.3% (0.5 s: 1.05, 91%, 8.6%). A
  reviewer simulated on all 207 (it plays each row to the next place, keeping who is
  who, and fixes where a mouse wears another label for most of half a second) takes
  the 108 videos with errors from 88.1% to 99.96% correct on average, 101 of them to
  >= 99.9%. In the app, after the image rows (2026-10-29, 193 videos, by minutes):
  94.03% -> 96.30% -> 99.97% (the list alone 99.98%); on 64 hard SLAP-2M views
  70.79% -> 81.38% -> 98.91% (alone 99.01%). Since every row that stands for a place
  offers the place's own fix, every video ends exactly at the simulated reviewer's
  plan, with no place it could not fix (before: 98.61% on SLAP, 17 places blocked).
  What is left is that plan itself: started from the labels the image rows leave, it
  ends lower on 5 videos (one SLAP view, 161055/backL, 93.91% vs 98.17% alone, is
  nearly all of the 0.10 points). An image row's Fix is accepted when it is right on
  the stretch it changes, by each mouse's first-seen label: the whole-video score
  drops for a right Fix while most of the video is swapped.
- **The simulated reviewer** (`~/luc3d-eval/oracle_lib.mjs` `playReviewN`, majority
  rule since 2026-10-28): a wrong label must hold for most of a 0.5 s stretch (one-frame
  flickers had made it swap and swap back), and a mouse not seen before the first place
  is met in that place's clip. Both change the measurement only.
- **Every close encounter instead** would be 0.5–1.8 rows a minute but long ones
  (median 3–16 s, p90 up to minutes), covering 34–80% of the video, which is why
  encounters are not on the list (the checks flag the ones worth a look).
- **Where a row lands:** the true switch is within the row's 2 s window in about
  70–80% of switches found (median 0–0.7 s off), so a Fix from the frame the
  reviewer stops on takes the right boundary; fixing from the row's own frame did
  about as well as from the exact switch frame in the simulation.
- **Its pair:** on 3–4 mice the row's pair was the swapped one in only a third to
  two thirds of the switches it found, so the Fix dialog lets the reviewer pick the
  two tracks (`ui/id-switch-modal.js`).

**Returns: an animal back from out of sight, for the coat-brightness check.**
`returnMoments(frames, out, fps, opts)` -> one moment `{frame, startFrame, identityA,
identityB, cues: ['return']}` per return of a track (its first detection after more
than `RETURN_DEFAULTS.gapSeconds` (1) unseen) and each other track, `startFrame` the
frame it was last seen, so `testMoments` reads the labels before the animal went out
of sight against the labels after it came back. `trackSingleCamera`'s `moments` =
candidate moments + returns (sorted); the review list does not take them (a place
per return would add 1–2.5 places a minute). Their rows note "an animal comes back
after being out of sight". They cost no decoding: the check reads its 4 Hz samples
anyway. Measured 2026-10-27 offline with the check's own code on precomputed coat
vectors (`~/luc3d-eval/extract_bright.mjs`, `eval_bright.mjs`), the simulated reviewer
working through the review list plus the check's rows:
- **SLAP original six (3–4 mice of different coats, 23 views):** 1,450 returns tested
  -> 13 rows, 11 at a real switch (2026-10-28: repeated returns after one swap share a
  row, and a near-tie and a return at one place are one row, `momentChangePoints`); the
  check's rows 13 (9 real) -> 21 (15 real); after review 99.75% -> 99.96% (150448/midL: two mice swapped from the start, put right
  while out of view, which the list had no place for: 96.0% -> 99.98%). In the app on
  that view: one return row, whose own Fix swaps 0:00.0–0:50.0 — 96.04% -> 99.86%.
  On 190807/top (brown, brown, white; Track All 66.5%) the app raised 5 return rows;
  accepting the 3 whose Fix made the labels right, as proposed, gave 85.0% (the other
  two: one changed nothing, one — renamed by an earlier Fix — would have hurt).
- **SLAP held-out (2 mice, 64 views):** 11 rows, 8 real; after review unchanged (99.90%).
- **sham_gdx (2 light-coated mice, 193 videos):** 2,464 returns -> 1 row, false;
  unchanged (99.97%). Coat brightness cannot tell look-alikes apart.
- **All of SLAP-2M (2026-10-28):** every multi-animal back/mid/top view (and its L
  twin) with proofread labels — 252 views, 42 sessions. 57 (all 2 mice) were set apart
  because SLEAP's own tracks in the raw file already equal the proofreading (the
  input-tracklet cue is then the answer): Track All 98.7%, the list takes them to 100%,
  no return rows. On the other 195 (1,632 min), after review with the list plus the
  check's rows: all 99.63% -> 99.77%; back pair 99.28% -> 99.55%, mid 99.77% -> 99.89%,
  top 99.87% (same); 2 mice 99.68% -> 99.82%, 3–4 mice 99.56% -> 99.67%; 50 return rows, 35 real, Fix as
  proposed helping 36 / hurting 13; 2 views better (10302022030623/backL 66.1% -> 100%,
  150448/midL 96.0% -> 99.98%), none worse.
- **Not adopted: search-zone stitching** after PrecisionTrack (Coulombe et al., 2025,
  "PrecisionTrack: A Platform for Automated Long-Term Social Behavior Analysis in
  Naturalized Environments", https://github.com/VincentCoulombe/precision_track): with
  the animal count known, a lost track's search zone grows ahead of where it was going
  and a returning detection takes the identity whose zone it comes back in. Reimplemented
  offline after Track All (`~/luc3d-eval/stitch.mjs`) and swept over its tuning ranges, it
  was neutral to worse (sham_gdx 94.08% -> 94.04%, 24 videos worse and 13 better; only
  SLAP six gained, at beta 1.5): mice come back far from where they left (handled, hidden),
  so where they were heading says little.

**Eager sessions only.** A lazy (> 150 MB) single-camera project is refused with
`SINGLE_CAMERA_LAZY_REASON` rather than tracked from its resident window. Track
Frame and Track Frame Range are refused on one camera (the tracker is temporal,
and a range would need its new tracks mapped onto the old ones).

**The image and size checks take none of the single-camera options (since #309 /
#310).** They score each contact label-free, before vs after (`scoreContacts`), and
have no runs of encounters, so this branch's single-camera image rule — a run
ending only above +|threshold| (`singleCameraImageContinueBelow`, removed in the
#309 merge) — went, and the options below apply to the coat-brightness check
only. That rule was measured on the image check's previous scoring (SLAP top/mid
false rows 71 -> 51; topC 93.8% -> 99.9% after both Fixes). Neither tests candidate
moments. The new image check on one camera is measured under `pose/id-switch-check.js`
("On one camera"): on the top and mid views of the 22 proofread SLAP sessions 91 rows,
2 false, 82 of 276 swaps found; on topC 5 rows, 3 false, both swaps found.

**Empty encounters are no evidence.** The coat-brightness check on one
camera runs with `skipEmpty` (`singleCameraCheckOptions`): runs ignore encounters scoring exactly
0, which had no samples on either side — 44.5% of the image check's encounters
on the 35 SLAP videos (2 samples a second; one camera's animals are seldom alone
for long), 24.4% of brightness's. Counted as "reads right", they started a
swapped stretch at the first encounter WITH evidence: a Fix from 0:04.2 for a
swap from 0:00, or from 0:25.9 when the pair's six encounters before it were all
empty. Skipping them: image rows 78 -> 68 (false 51 -> 42), 18 of 59 swaps caught
either way, rows whose Fix has a wrong far edge 4 -> 2, rows fully undone (with
the boundary set by the user) 9 -> 11; brightness rows 21 -> 18 (false 7 -> 4),
14 caught either way. The two far edges left are chained swaps — a pair's stretch starting
where ANOTHER pair swapped, or a three-animal rotation — which one pair's rows
cannot see.

**Key exports.** `singleCameraName(session)` (the camera, or null);
`singleCameraTrackerConfig(numAnimals, {windowSize, oksStddev, connectBreaks})`;
`trackSingleCamera(session, cfg, {onProgress, signal, fps, look, filter, duplicates})` ->
`{numIdentities, frames, tracked, untracked, filtered, duplicates, moments, review}`; `candidateMoments(frames,
out, inputTracks, ambiguous, look, fps, extra?)` (`extra`: more candidates `{f, a, b,
cue}` to cluster with them); `CANDIDATE_DEFAULTS` (`ambiguityMargin`
0.1, `trackletGapFrames` 5, `clusterSeconds` 3); `handoverCandidates(frames, out, fps,
opts)` -> `[{f, a, b, cue: 'handover'}]`; `HANDOVER_DEFAULTS` (`lookSeconds` 1,
`nearBody` 0.5); `returnMoments(frames, out, fps, opts)` -> `[{frame, startFrame, identityA,
identityB, cues: ['return']}]`; `RETURN_DEFAULTS` (`gapSeconds` 1); `reviewResult(moments, session, fps)` -> the review list's result; `singleCameraCheckSession(session)`
-> stand-in | null | `{fail}`; `swapSingleCameraIdentities(session, from, to, idA, idB)`
-> `{frames, tracks: boolean}` | null; `singleCameraCheckOptions(cue)` -> the options
the coat-brightness check runs with on one camera (`{skipEmpty: true,
clearestEndLeads: true}`); `DUPLICATE_DEFAULTS` (`iou` 0.5, `oks` 0.6, `oksStddev`
0.1, `minNodes` 3); `duplicateDetections(dets, o)` -> the indices of one frame's
duplicate detections; `boxIou(a, b)`; `duplicateOks(strong, weak, strongBox,
stddev, minNodes)`; `SINGLE_CAMERA_LAZY_REASON`. In `trackSingleCamera`,
`opts.filter` is the Wizard's three minimums (absent, or all 0 = off) and
`opts.duplicates` the DUPLICATE_DEFAULTS overrides (absent or null = off);
`filtered` and `duplicates` count what each left out.

**Imports from project modules.** `pose/sleap-tracker.js`,
`pose/detection-filter-stats.js` (`passesDetectionFilter`).

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js` (Track All), `ui/id-switch-modal.js` (the
checks and the fix).

**Coverage.** `tests/test-single-camera-tracking.mjs` (one track per animal
across 18 input tracklet breaks, the spurious detection and the user frame, the
candidate moments, the stand-in and a planted swap found by the real size check,
the track swap and its fallback, the refusals, §6/§7 `skipEmpty` and
`clearestEndLeads` on the classifier's change points, and §9 the review list:
handovers, its rows, their Fix plan and the saved checklist, and §11 the
detection filters: each minimum leaves its failing detections without a track,
counted as `filtered` — the Wizard's own count — all 0 changes nothing, and
duplicates are looked for after the filters) and
`tests/e2e/single-camera-track-all.mjs` (the real buttons, the refusals, a planted
swap found, fixed and undone through the ID Switches tab, a swap away from every
contact found by the coat-brightness check at a candidate moment, a "Both" row of
size and brightness, §6 which checks take the single-camera options, §7b a Wizard
filter counted in the Wizard, then left without a track by Track All and counted
in the status and the summary box, §7c the Instances tab's mean node score, and §8 the
review list after Track All: counted in the status and the summary box, listed in
the tab, and a review row's Fix between two picked tracks, from here to the end,
then Fix again from past its own window: only the stretch between stays swapped;
then a check row on the same place: both Fixes offered, the check's taken, then the
place's Fix again; then an image row shown with a coat-brightness return, not on the
list: once its Fix is taken, Fix again is the return's, from here to the end).
`tests/e2e/_real-single-camera.mjs` runs the whole thing on a real SLEAP file
through File ▸ Load SLP.

---

### pose/temporal-smoothing.js

**Purpose.** Post-triangulation temporal filtering of the 3D tracks — the
smoothing half of luc3d #134 ("3d projection would benefit from anipose's
temporal smoothing and RPE flags"). The RPE half of that issue already shipped
as the Tracking Wizard's `reprojErrorThreshold` in `triangulateAndReproject`
(`pose/triangulation.js`). Reads and writes `InstanceGroup.points3d` ONLY;
`Instance._xy` (`'user'` and `'predicted'` 2D) is never modified.

**Where it sits.** AFTER triangulation, never inside tracking, and never on 2D.
Pipeline: track → proofread identities → triangulate → smooth. Smoothing before
identities are proofread smooths ACROSS an ID swap, welding two animals'
trajectories together; `CrossViewTracker` is causal and frame-by-frame while a
smoother is non-causal; and the tracker's hyperparameters are measured against
benchmark corpora that changing its 3D state would invalidate. 2D filtering was
rejected because the low-acceleration prior is physically true in 3D and only
approximately true under perspective projection, and because interpolating a
missing view fabricates an observation that then votes in triangulation as if
it were real data.

**Algorithm.** Two independently switchable stages over each node's 1D
coordinate signals (15 nodes = 45 signals per identity; nodes never enter each
other's windows):
1. **Median** (despike) — nonlinear, rank-based, removes impulses.
2. **Gaussian** (smooth) — weighted mean, `sigma = (window-1)/6`, removes
   broadband noise.
Order is fixed: Gaussian-first would smear an isolated outlier across its
window, after which the median can no longer remove it. Windows are coerced to
ODD (`normalizeWindow`) so they stay centered — an even window is a half-frame
phase shift against the video.

**Missing data.** Two regimes, deliberately distinguished. ISOLATED HOLES (gap
<= `smoothMaxGap`) are skipped with the Gaussian weights renormalized over
present samples. SEGMENT BOUNDARIES (longer gaps, and the ends of the
trajectory) shrink the window SYMMETRICALLY — a one-sided window is an
extrapolation, biased toward whichever side still has data by roughly
`velocity x half-window` (~15 mm at 10 mm/frame with a 7-window), which reads
as the skeleton lurching at every occlusion edge. Presence is decided ONCE at
the SKELETON level and applied to all 45 signals, because a single 1D signal
cannot distinguish "occluded in 4 of 5 cameras" from "the animal left", and
per-node segmentation would deform the skeleton. Gaps are NEVER filled — a NaN
input is a NaN output — unlike anipose's `filter_3d.py`, which `np.interp`s
across gaps of any length before filtering. This also keeps the gap structure
invariant across the two stages, so segments are computed once and reused.

**Not a lazy sweep.** `session.instanceGroups` is fully resident
(`sweepLazyFrameWindows` releases `frameGroups`, never `instanceGroups`, and
the SLP 2.8 columnar reader materializes every group's 3D on load — luc3d
#189), so this walks the map directly and hydrates no 2D. It therefore cannot
fall into the resident-only bug class of luc3d #194/#195.

**Provenance.** Sets `InstanceGroup.smoothed = true` and clears the derived
reprojection caches (`group.reprojections`; `group.reprojectedInstances` goes back
to the shared `NO_REPROJECTED_INSTANCES`, never `clear()`ed), which refill lazily.
It also marks the written array (`markPoints3dSmoothed`), so that refill
reprojects the smoothed 3D instead of re-solving the 2D — without the mark,
groups with no `triangulationMethod` (Track All's) or any group under a
reprojection-error threshold drew the unsmoothed answer.

**Runs replace, never stack.** The mark keeps a pooled copy of the group's 3D
from before its first smoothing (taken just before the first write; the
`slice()` matters, since `pooledPoints3d` returns an already-pooled array as-is),
and the gather reads that copy (`unsmoothedPoints3d`), so window 60 then
window 3 gives window 3's result. `unsmoothSession(session, {writeFrames})`
copies it back and drops the mark (the "both windows 0" action);
`hasSmoothed3d(session, frameIdx?)` says whether any group (on a frame, or
anywhere) is smoothed; the session-wide answer walks only the frames a pass
wrote (a per-session `WeakMap` set), dropping each one found no longer
smoothed, because `ui/smoothing-button.js` asks on every overlay draw.
Saved with the project: the source goes in the file and comes back marked
(`import-export/smoothing-metadata.js`), and a reader registers the frames it
re-marked with `noteSmoothedFrames(session, frames)` so the button finds them.
`triangulationMethod` is deliberately UNTOUCHED: smoothing is
orthogonal to which solver produced the points (a group can be BA-solved AND
smoothed), and a third value there would erase the solver identity
`resolveTriangulationMethod` exists to preserve.

**Exports.** `normalizeWindow`, `sigmaForWindow`, `buildSegments`, `medianOf`,
`smoothSignalInto`, `resolveSmoothingParams`, `collectTrajectories`,
`smoothTrajectory`, `smoothSession`, `smoothSessionAsync`, `smoothFrame`,
`unsmoothSession`, `hasSmoothed3d`, `noteSmoothedFrames`, `removeSmoothing`,
`restoreSmoothing`, `hasRemovedSmoothing`. **Remove / Restore smoothing**:
`removeSmoothing(session, {scope, config})` records the filter's whole input
(`smoothingInputPrint`: every group with 3D in walk order, its frame,
identity and `points3d` array in flat arrays; a smoothed group's
pre-smoothing copy, otherwise a hash of its values), copies the smoothed
values into ONE `Float64Array`, then `unsmoothSession`s. Kept per session in
a `WeakMap`, in memory only (never saved). `restoreSmoothing(session, params)`
puts them back, re-marking each group with its kept source, only when the
settings resolve the same (`config`) and the input is unchanged
(`inputUnchanged`: same groups, arrays, frames, identities, and values equal
to the kept sources / hashes) — a smoothed value reads its neighbours' frames,
so any change anywhere makes the kept values suspect. Else it writes nothing
and returns `stale` with the scope and frames to smooth again. Either way the
copy is dropped; any new run (`smoothSession*`, `smoothFrame`,
`unsmoothSession`) drops it too. 400,000 poses x 15 nodes: remove ~0.3 s,
restore ~0.2 s, vs ~2.9 s to smooth. `smoothFrame(session, params, frameIdx)`
is Temporal Smoothing ▸ This frame: it writes exactly (bit for bit) what
`smoothSession` writes on that frame, but reads only the frames that value
depends on — within median + Gaussian radius, plus a walk out to the frame's
segment edges (absent runs > `maxGap`), stopped once a present frame lies
`max(reach, minSegment)` out, where the true edge can no longer change the
result. It filters that slice with `smoothSignalInto`, passing the true segment
in slice coordinates and `from`/`to` so only the samples the frame reads are
computed (the sums run in the same order as the full pass). Identity bucketing
matches `collectTrajectories`; the one whole-session walk
(`hasAmbiguousNoIdentity`) runs only when the frame has an unidentified group.
`skipped` counts that frame's groups. `unsmoothSession` with `writeFrames`
walks only those frames. `smoothSessionAsync(session, params, {writeFrames, onProgress})`
awaits `onProgress(done, total)` (poses) after each node coordinate of each
track; both entry points run the same generators (`smoothTrajectorySteps`,
`smoothSessionSteps`), so the result and the writes are identical, and nothing
of a trajectory is written before its last step.

**Imports.** `pose/pose-data.js` (`POINT3D_STRIDE`, `points3dNodeCount`,
`NO_REPROJECTED_INSTANCES`, `pooledPoints3d`, `markPoints3dSmoothed`,
`isPoints3dSmoothed`, `unsmoothedPoints3d`, `clearPoints3dSmoothed`) only —
no UI imports, so the module stays unit-testable in plain Node.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`smoothSessionAsync`, `smoothFrame`,
`unsmoothSession`, `resolveSmoothingParams`, `hasSmoothed3d`,
`removeSmoothing`, `restoreSmoothing`), `ui/smoothing-button.js`
(`hasSmoothed3d`, `hasRemovedSmoothing`),
`import-export/smoothing-metadata.js` (`hasSmoothed3d`, `noteSmoothedFrames`).

**Tests.** `tests/test-temporal-smoothing.mjs` (`smoothFrame` is checked bit
for bit against the all-frames pass on every frame of noisy fixtures with
holes, segment edges, duplicates and unidentified groups, over 270
configurations); save/reopen in `tests/e2e/smoothing-save-reopen.mjs`. Bench:
`tests/e2e/_bench-smoothing-this-frame.mjs` (Node) and
`tests/e2e/_bench-smoothing-ui.mjs` (menu click to status, eager + lazy). Verified by
mutation: removing the symmetric shrink, zero-padding instead of renormalizing,
and filling gaps each fail with the specific artifact they represent.

---

### pose/run-state.js

**Purpose.** Has Track or Triangulate been run on a session, and does its result
still stand? Drives the blue Track and Triangulate toolbar buttons
(`ui/run-buttons.js`). A run is noted when it finishes; its result stands while
the session shows it: Track while some instance group has an identity,
Triangulate while some group still holds a 3D array the run produced or kept.
Arrays are marked by reference (a `WeakSet`), so anything that replaces a
group's 3D (Track All's own 3D, a re-solve outside a run, a skeleton edit)
drops that group out, while a node move's re-solve keeps it
(`carryTriangulateMark`); smoothing writes in place and keeps it. In memory
only: a reopened project starts with neither noted (the user chose this over
saving it).

Asked on every overlay draw, so each answer keeps a WITNESS (frame + group that
showed the result) and walks `session.instanceGroups` only when the witness
stops holding: it finds a new one or forgets the run, after which nothing is
walked until the next run.

**Exports.** `noteTrackRun(session)`, `noteTriangulateRun(session, frames)`
(marks the 3D of every group on `frames`, null = all, that has a
`triangulationMethod` and valid 3D, solved or kept by the run),
`carryTriangulateMark(old, new)`, `hasTrackResult(session)`,
`hasTriangulateResult(session)`.

**Imports.** `./pose-data.js` (`someValidPoint3d`). **Imported by**
`pose/triangulation.js`, `pose/tracker.js`, `ui/export-modals.js`,
`ui/run-buttons.js`. **Tests.** `tests/test-run-state.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/run-buttons-blue.mjs`.

### pose/triangulation.js

**Purpose.** DLT triangulation, bundle-adjustment refinement, reprojection
math, fundamental-matrix / epipolar utilities, Hungarian assignment. Also
hosts the lazy-H5 frame loader and the user-facing triangulation orchestration
(single-frame, all-frames, multi-frame range).

**The pure math lives in `pose/triangulation-core.js`** (DLT, refinement,
reprojection, errors, `triangulateAndReproject`, `invert3x3`, and the tracker's
geometry: `hungarianAlgorithm`, `cameraCenter`, `backProjectToRay(s)`,
`pointToRayDistance`, `pointsToRayDistances`, `computeFundamentalMatrix`,
`epipolarError`, `epipolarErrorMatrix`) so the Triangulate All worker pool and
Track All's tracker worker can load it; this module imports it and
**re-exports every public name**, so all existing imports keep working. It
installs the core's two settings hooks (`setTriangulationSettingsHooks`) with
`isCameraTracked` / `getTrackingThreshold`.

**Performance (outputs bit-identical — see
`tests/test-triangulation-kernels.mjs` and the `_bench-progress-overlay.mjs
DIGEST_OPS=1` digests).** `cameraCenter` and `backProjectToRay(s)` are memoized
per P array (`_pGeometry` / `_pseudoInverse`, a WeakMap) and VALIDATED on every
hit against a snapshot of P's 12 entries, so an in-place-mutated P recomputes;
returned centres are copies. Triangulate All's per-group step is split into
`_prepareGroupStep` (camera fix-ups, >=2-usable-views gate, `usedCameras`) /
solve / `_applyGroupStep`, so `triangulateAllFrames` (eager and the windowed
`sweepTriangulateAllFrames`) submit solves to `pose/triangulation-pool.js` and
apply results in submission order; `_triangulateGroupStep` (prepare + inline
solve + apply) remains for Triangulate Range.

**`triangulatePointDLT` is frame-invariant, and that is load-bearing** — see
`pose/triangulation-core.js`, which is where it now lives.

**3D points are flat.** The array-level entry points speak the
`Float64Array(3N)` `points3d` representation (see `pose/pose-data.js`):
`triangulatePoints` and `triangulatePointsBA` **return** one, and
`reprojectPoints`, `reprojectPointsCamera`, `pointsToRayDistances` and
`triangulateAndReproject` (via `result.points3d`) **consume** one. The
*per-point* helpers — `triangulatePointDLT`, `triangulatePointBA`,
`reprojectPoint`, `reprojectPointCamera`, `pointToRayDistance` — still take and
return boxed `[x,y,z]` / `[x,y]` triples (transient scratch, not storage). Use
`readPoint3d(pts, k, buf)` to feed them without allocating per node.

Reprojection *outputs* stay boxed `[x,y]|null` per node: negligible PER FRAME
only — the windowed Triangulate All must not retain them project-wide (below).

**THE bulk-sweep primitive: `sweepLazyFrameWindows`.** Exported from this module
and used by EVERY operation that must touch every frame. Hydrate a 2,000-frame
window (`batchLoadLazyFrames`) → run the callback → drop the window's non-user
`frameGroups` (pinning the on-screen frame and any user-edited frame) →
`releaseWindow` → force a real collection every 5 windows. `opts.start`/`opts.end`
restrict it to a range. Contract points:
- **Playback eviction is HELD for the whole sweep** (`holdLazyResidency`,
  released in a `finally` by the exported wrapper around
  `_sweepLazyFrameWindowsHeld`): eviction protects windows around the on-screen
  frame, not the window being swept, so a draw in one of the sweep's yields
  could otherwise drop frames the sweep has not visited yet.
- **The window release also gives each released frame's group members' 2D back
  to the store** (`releaseFrameMembers2d`, `pose/lazy-residency.js`); otherwise
  one sweep leaves every member of the project holding a private copy of its 2D.
  Solver jobs capture their 2D at submit, so releasing a window whose solves are
  still in flight is safe.
- **Release lag** (Track All's tracker worker): `opts.releaseLag` N keeps the
  last N visited windows resident and releases each only after the next N have
  been visited, awaiting `opts.beforeRelease(start, end)` first — the tracker's
  results are WRITTEN into the window's FrameGroups, so unlike a solve they must
  land before it goes. Both omitted: no lag.
- **Progress is clock-paced** (`createProgressPacer`): `opts.onProgress(done,
  total)` is awaited before each ~250 ms yield and once at the end, `done` being
  the sweep POSITION (frames passed, data or not), so it rises monotonically to
  `total`; `opts.onLoadProgress(done, total)` reports the non-windowed branch's
  up-front `loadAllLazyFrames` stage (`opts.onStatus(msg)` is the text-only
  fallback).
`sweepTrackAllFrames` (`pose/tracker.js`), `ui/export-modals.js`'s sweeps and
`sweepTriangulateAllFrames` all delegate to it.

**Which frames it visits (`_hasFrameData`) — a hydrated `FrameGroup` OR an
`instanceGroups` entry, never `FrameGroup` alone.** `onFrame(frameIdx, fg)`
therefore receives `fg === undefined` for a frame that has 3D grouping but no
resident 2D, and a callback that dereferences it must guard (`exportLabels` does;
the triangulation callbacks read `instanceGroups` and do not care). Gating on
`session.frameGroups.get(fi)` alone skips every frame whose 2D did not come back
on hydration, while Triangulate All has already wiped `reprojections`
project-wide. The e2e harness is structurally blind to it (its fixture gives
every frame 2D in every camera); `tests/test-sweep-frame-coverage.mjs` pins the
union rule for both the windowed and eager branches.

Rule of thumb: a `for (... of session.frameGroups)` loop in a BULK operation is a
bug. That map holds only RESIDENT frames, so such a loop silently processes
~nothing and returns a plausible count. The opposite mistake is
`loadAllLazyFrames` + iterate, which materializes 2D for every frame × camera at
once and OOMs the renderer. `sweepLazyFrameWindows` is the shape that is neither.

**IMPORTANT — mutations do not survive the sweep.** After each window, non-user
frames are dropped and rebuilt from the columnar store on next hydration, so
mutating a PREDICTED instance's fields inside `onFrame` is lost. Durable edits must
land in the store's own columns (see `SioLazyLoader.remapTracksFromIdentity`),
in `frameIdentityMap`, or in `instanceGroups` — or must mark the instance
user-edited so its frame is pinned. This is why the track-swap fixes
(`ui/identity-assignment.js`) write the store rather than sweeping.

**A store write must be mirrored into `instanceGroups`, not just `frameGroups`.**
`frameGroups` holds only the resident window, while `instanceGroups` is rebuilt
PROJECT-WIDE at reopen by `reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy`, and its
members are lightweight placeholders whose `trackIdx` was copied from the store
then and is never refreshed on hydration. Group-level operations read that copy
(`track-identity-ops.js` `deleteTrackAt` decides which groups to DISSOLVE from
it), so a store rewrite that updates only resident frames makes the next track
delete dissolve the wrong groups. `swapTracks` and `swapAssignTrack` therefore
pair each `swapTracksInStore` with a `swapTracksInMemory` pass over BOTH maps,
sharing one `seen` set: the two maps share instance objects for hydrated frames,
and swapping such an instance twice silently restores it.

**Triangulate All is windowed on a lazy project.** `triangulateAllFrames`
dispatches on the same `windowed` capability check `trackAll` uses
(`lazyLoader.isSync && typeof releaseWindow === 'function'`). With a windowing
loader it runs `sweepTriangulateAllFrames` over `sweepLazyFrameWindows`. Without
hydration a reopened project's group members are null-filled placeholders and
almost every group is skipped. `loadAllLazyFrames` is deliberately NOT used —
that is the OOM the memory work exists to prevent.

**Neither Triangulate All path keeps derived state.** Both clear every group's
reprojections and `state.triangulationResults` up front
(`dropAllDerivedTriangulationState`, exported for Group by Track) and store only
`points3d`, `usedCameras`, the method and the error stats, asking the pool for
`summaryOnly` results — keeping every group's reprojections and per-camera errors
was enough (+1.9 GB on a large eager project) to run Refined out of memory. The
display derives a shown frame's from its 3D (`ui/rendering.js`'s reprojection
fill, `reprojectStoredPoints3d`) and keeps a bounded number. Its timeline pass
walks the frames it triangulated rather than `state.triangulationResults`.
Covered by `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-bounded-derived-state.mjs`.

**Nor does any other bulk path:**
- **Tracks ▸ Group by Track** (`ui/export-modals.js`) calls
  `dropAllDerivedTriangulationState` before its sweep and stores no
  reprojections or results.
- **Triangulate Range** (`triangulateMultiFrameInstances`) drops a frame's
  derived state (`dropDerivedReprojections`) when it re-solves a group there,
  and leaves every other frame's alone: the range need not be the project.
Each one's timeline pass walks its own list of frames (the frames Group by
Track regrouped, the range's `result.frames`), not `state.triangulationResults`.
Covered by `tests/e2e/bulk-triangulation-bounded-derived-state.mjs`.

On the windowed path `points3d` is deliberately *not* wiped up front but replaced
per group as the sweep reaches it, so an interrupted sweep never leaves groups
with no 3D. `_prepareGroupStep` / `_applyGroupStep` (and `_triangulateGroupStep`)
hold the camera-name fixup + `>=2`-usable-view gate + store shared by both paths
so they cannot drift; `_fgHasUserInstances` and `_encourageGC` are
module-private helpers of `sweepLazyFrameWindows`.

**Triangulation methods.** `'dlt'` (default) is the fast linear DLT.
`'ba'` initializes from DLT then runs a per-point Levenberg–Marquardt refinement
of the geometric reprojection error, cameras fixed — aniposelib's
`CameraGroup.optim_points`, what sleap-anipose runs for pose triangulation.
Costs ~5x DLT per keypoint.

**`'ba'` is guaranteed never worse than DLT on the reported error.** Three
properties make that true, documented at length above `BA_ROBUST_SCALE_PX` in
`pose/triangulation-core.js`:
1. *Residual space.* Residuals are in the camera's **native (distorted)** pixel
   space — where the detections live and where `meanError` is reported.
   `distortJacobian` supplies the analytic Brown–Conrady derivative and
   `projectAndJacobianCamera` chain-rules it onto the projection Jacobian.
2. *Robust loss.* Soft-L1 (pseudo-Huber) via IRLS, at aniposelib's default scale
   (`BA_ROBUST_SCALE_PX` = 15 px). `robustScale: Infinity` restores plain squares.
3. *Metric-matching polish.* A second LM phase minimizes Σ‖r‖ — the reported
   mean error up to 1/nViews — seeded from whichever of {DLT, phase 1} scores
   better; native-space soft-L1 alone still regressed. Disable with
   `polish: false`. A backtracking `guard` (on by default) is the net for what
   phase 2 cannot cover.

Consequences: the L1-type objective is ~10% less efficient than L2 on clean
Gaussian noise but an order of magnitude better under a gross outlier.
`meanErrorUndistorted` is deliberately **not** guarded — DLT is inherently
favored in ideal-pinhole coordinates, and guarding both vetoed real
improvements. Views **excluded** from the solve still count toward `meanError`,
so BA can raise it; the invariant is pinned over the solve's own views.
`{ robustScale: Infinity, polish: false, guard: false }` reproduces the old
behavior, which `tests/test-triangulation-ba.js` uses as a baseline. The LM
ladder itself was verified monotone and was not changed.

**No joint bundle adjustment: cameras are NEVER refined (deliberate scope).** The
`'ba'` method is non-linear triangulation with fixed cameras. A joint
camera+structure solve was implemented and **deleted**; do not re-add it. LUCID
consumes a calibration; calibration belongs where it is produced (sleap-anipose,
on a checkerboard). The reasons — the calibration is an input the user trusts,
metric scale is unobservable from animal keypoints, and reprojection error would
stop being a diagnostic — are spelled out in the "LUCID DOES NON-LINEAR
TRIANGULATION ONLY" comment in `pose/triangulation-core.js`.

Naming note: the user-facing label is **"Refined" / "Ref"** (Settings ▸ Default
Triangulation shows "Refined (Ref)"; the Triangulate / Triangulate All dropdowns
show "Ref"), not "Bundle Adjustment", which wrongly implied camera refinement.
The method key is still `'ba'` in `options.method`,
`group.triangulationMethod`, `localStorage`, and the per-group
`metadata.lucid.triangulationMethod` written to the `.slp`.

The method is selected via `options.method` on `triangulateAndReproject` and
threaded through the orchestration functions; the chosen method is recorded on
each group (`group.triangulationMethod`) and in each `state.triangulationResults`
entry (`.method`) so the info panel can label it.

**`options.method`'s default is SILENT.** Omitting it means DLT, not "keep the
group's method". The rule is **exported 3D == displayed 3D**: no path may replace
a Refined solve with a DLT one behind the user's back. Three helpers:

- `resolveTriangulationMethod(group)` → `'ba'|'dlt'`. The group's own recorded
  method wins; otherwise Settings ▸ Triangulation Method
  (`getDefaultTriangulationMethod`). Never a bare `'dlt'` literal. Used by
  `reTriangulateGroup`, both grouping sweeps in `ui/export-modals.js`,
  `ui/sessions-panes.js`'s move-view re-triangulate and `ui/ui-wiring.js`'s
  environment-skeleton solve. **Deliberately NOT used by `ui/rendering.js`'s
  lazy reprojection fill:** it re-derives reprojections for 3D it does not own,
  so it must REPRODUCE the recorded solve exactly. Rule of thumb — *writes*
  `points3d` → `resolveTriangulationMethod`; only *reads* it → match
  `triangulationMethod` exactly.
- `findEquivalentPriorGroup(priorGroups, group)` → the prior group with identical
  membership (camera for camera, by `Instance` object identity), or null.
- `adoptPrior3d(group, prior, method)` → copies `points3d`,
  `triangulationMethod` and `usedCameras`; returns true when `group` therefore
  needs no solve. Requires identical membership **AND**
  `prior.triangulationMethod === method`, so it only fires when re-solving would
  be a provable no-op (an *unknown* prior method never matches; smoothed 3D,
  `isPoints3dSmoothed`, never matches either). Does **not**
  copy `reprojections` — leaving them empty is what lets `ui/rendering.js`'s fill
  regenerate them (and `state.triangulationResults`) with the adopted method.

**The selected method GOVERNS the two grouping sweeps.** With the default set to
Refined, "Group by Track / Group by ID & Triangulate All" leave Refined 3D on
*every* group — including unchanged-but-previously-DLT ones, which is why
`adoptPrior3d` takes a `method` (adopting on membership alone would make the
setting a silent no-op). An explicit pick beats the default —
`groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll(explicitMethod)`, and "Triangulate All ▸ DLT"
passes `'dlt'`. With DLT selected, a grouping op over Refined 3D *does* re-solve
it as DLT — the setting being obeyed, and named in the progress text and status
line. Guarded by `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-ba-display.mjs` (the panel's label
and value agree) and `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-ba-export.mjs` (the exported 3D
is the Refined solve), on `tests/e2e/fixtures/ba-rig-fixture.js`.

The sweeps ADOPT rather than re-solve when membership is unchanged, which makes
the correct behavior cheaper than re-solving every group, and report 3D
provenance ("N kept existing 3D, N solved via Refined, N via DLT") in their
progress text and status line. The invariant — *every* call site states its
method, by resolving it — is enforced by
`tests/test-triangulation-method-propagation.mjs`, a source scan that fails,
naming file and line, on any call that omits the options object, omits `method`,
hardcodes `'dlt'`, or passes something that is neither a resolver nor a
threaded-in method. A scan rather than a runtime assert, because it covers every
call site, not only the paths something executes.

**Distortion handling.** 2D keypoints on disk are lens-distorted. **DLT** runs in
ideal pinhole space: observations are undistorted first (`Camera.undistortPoint`),
which is required — DLT is linear only in those coordinates. **BA does not**; it
refines against the raw native-space detections, so `triangulateAndReproject`
keeps `allObservationsRaw` index-parallel to the undistorted set and masks the
two identically whenever the outlier-rejection loop drops a view. Reprojections
meant for display or error comparison must be **re-distorted** back to native
pixel space (`reprojectPointCamera` / `reprojectPointsCamera` → project, then
`Camera.distortPoint`); comparing ideal reprojections against raw keypoints
produces spurious error growing toward the frame edges.

`triangulateAndReproject` reports the reprojection error in **both** spaces:
`meanError`/`errors` (distorted — what is drawn and broken down per view/node)
and `meanErrorUndistorted`/`errorsUndistorted` (ideal pinhole, a diagnostic). The
info panel shows the distorted value as the headline ("N.NN px", colour-coded)
with "undist N.NN px" as a subtitle; the per-view and per-node breakdowns remain
distorted-space. Both error spaces are recomputed on project load — `.slp`
projects in `slp-import.js` and JSON/v2/v3 projects in `save-load.js`
(`_restoreProjectV2`) — so the subtitle is populated for loaded projects too.

**Key exports.**
- BA math: `triangulatePointBA(observations, projMatrices, initial?, options?)`
  (`options`: `cameras` → native-space residuals and `observations` are then the
  RAW detections; `robustScale` px, default `BA_ROBUST_SCALE_PX`; `polish`;
  `guard`; `maxIterations`; `tol`),
  `triangulatePointsBA(allObservations, projMatrices, initialPoints?, options?)`
  (forwards `options` verbatim), `BA_ROBUST_SCALE_PX` (= 15),
  `triangulationMethodLabel(method)` → `'DLT'` | `'Refined'`.
  Module-private: `distortJacobian(camera, ideal)` → 2x2 Brown–Conrady
  derivative, `projectAndJacobianCamera(point, camera)` → native-space
  projection + 2x3 Jacobian.
- Math: `triangulatePointDLT`, `triangulatePoints`, `reprojectPoint`,
  `reprojectPoints` (ideal pinhole), `reprojectPointCamera` /
  `reprojectPointsCamera` (project then re-distort into the camera's native
  pixel space — use these whenever reprojections are compared against or drawn
  over raw keypoints), `computeReprojectionError`,
  `computeReprojectionErrors`, `computeMeanReprojectionError`,
  `computeInstanceDistance(pointsA, pointsB, weights?)` (optional per-node
  `weights` → weighted mean distance; weight-0 nodes ignored; omitted ⇒ all 1),
  `computeInstanceDistanceTo(pointsA, instB, weights?)` (same, reading `instB`'s
  points directly; what `pose/tracker.js` uses), `cameraDepth`,
  `reprojectStoredPoints3d`, `hungarianAlgorithm`, `cameraCenter`,
  `invert3x3`, `backProjectToRay`, `backProjectToRays`,
  `pointToRayDistance`, `pointsToRayDistances`,
  `computeFundamentalMatrix`, `epipolarError`, `epipolarErrorMatrix`.
- Plane fitting (View ▸ Define Planes):
  `fitPlaneToPoints3d(points3d)` → `{centroid, normal, rms, nPoints}|null` —
  **defined in `pose/plane-fit.js` and merely RE-EXPORTED here**, so
  `pose/plane-data.js` can reach it without importing this UI-coupled module;
  `jacobiEigen` moved with it and is imported back for the DLT solver. Also
  `projectPoints3dOntoPlane(points3d, plane)` → a NEW flat `points3d` with every
  present point dropped onto the plane (input untouched, missing nodes stay
  missing), which stays here. The fit is **total least squares via PCA** — the
  normal is the eigenvector of the smallest eigenvalue of the points' 3x3
  covariance, via `jacobiEigen` (which returns eigenvectors as ROWS paired
  with unsorted `eigenvalues[i]`). Perpendicular distance, because the corners
  carry error in all three axes. Returns **null** for fewer than 3 points,
  coincident points, or COLLINEAR points (detected via the middle eigenvalue
  relative to the largest) — a normal there would be arbitrary.
- CONSTRAINED plane fitting (IMMUTABLE / "frozen" plane nodes):
  `fitPlaneConstrained(points3d, options)`,
  `projectPoints3dOntoPlaneConstrained(points3d, plane, immutable)`,
  `mergeFrozenPoints3d(solved, frozen, immutable)`,
  `summarizePlaneTriangulation(points3d, nodeErrors, immutable)`,
  `planesInvalidatedByFit(movedNodeIds, planes, excludePlaneId)`,
  `orientNormalLike(normal, previous)`, `normalizeImmutableMask(immutable, n)`,
  and the constants `PLANE_COPLANAR_TOL_FRAC` (1e-3), `PLANE_COPLANAR_TOL_FLOOR`,
  `PLANE_ANCHOR_COND_FRAC` (κ = 1e-3), `PLANE_ANCHOR_NOISE_FACTOR` (1e3),
  `PLANE_MUTABLE_DEVIATION_FRAC` (0.05).
  All PURE and model-agnostic: they take a flat `points3d` plus a plain
  immutability spec (`Set` of indices | boolean mask | index list, normalized by
  `normalizeImmutableMask`), never the plane node-pool objects. An immutable
  node's 3D is FROZEN, so the fit minimizes the same perpendicular residual
  subject to hard linear constraints: an anchor pins the offset and the normal
  is restricted to an admissible subspace, with the mutable points **scattered
  about the anchor, not the centroid**. The frozen set's rank decides it: rank 0
  (1 anchor, or coincident) ⇒ 3-D normal search; rank 1 (2 anchors, or 3+
  collinear) ⇒ a closed-form 2×2 eigenproblem; rank 2 (3+ non-collinear) ⇒ the
  anchors alone determine the plane and the mutable points get **zero** weight.
  Exactly 3 non-collinear anchors use the exact cross product; 4+ are free-fit
  first and rejected unless `rms ≤ max(τ·anchorDiameter, floor)`.
  **The 0-immutable case deliberately does NOT come through here** — it returns
  `code: 'not_constrained'` and the caller must use `fitPlaneToPoints3d`, whose
  floats are pinned by `tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs`.
  **Every threshold is SCALE-RELATIVE** (a fraction of the point set's
  diameter): scene units are whatever the calibration used.
  **Result object** — `{ok, code, message, plane, anchorIndices, anchorNames,
  mutableIndices, mutableNames, warnings[], metrics}`. `code` is
  machine-readable so the UI decides block-vs-confirm **structurally, never by
  sniffing the message**: `ok` | `not_constrained` | `no_anchor_3d` |
  `anchors_collinear` | `anchors_noncoplanar` | `underdetermined`. `warnings[]`
  carries the non-fatal half — `anchors_collinear_relaxed` (a 3rd+ collinear
  anchor relaxes to the 2-anchor line case), `anchors_coincident`, and
  `mutable_far_from_plane` (the UI should confirm, not block). Messages are keyed
  to node NAMES with the plane name, never indices.
  **Fails before mutating**: validation returns without reading or writing
  anything further, and the flatten is a separate call.
  **`hasPoint3d` only rejects NaN**, so anchors are checked with an explicit
  `isFinite` (an ±Infinity anchor would reach `jacobiEigen`, which has no guard)
  and return `no_anchor_3d`.
  `projectPoints3dOntoPlaneConstrained` copies immutable coordinates through
  **bit-identically** (a raw element copy — projecting an on-plane point is not
  a no-op under round-off, and a frozen point drifting an ulp per re-fit is the
  exact corruption the flag exists to prevent).
  `summarizePlaneTriangulation` reports `nNodes`/`meanError` over MUTABLE nodes
  only and the frozen residuals separately as `nAnchors`/`anchorMeanError` plus a
  per-node `provenance` (`'frozen'`|`'triangulated'`|`'missing'`): a frozen
  node's residual is out-of-sample, so folding it into `meanError` misrepresents
  the solve. `planesInvalidatedByFit` does not block a shared-node fit; it tells
  the UI whose `planeFit` to drop after a fit moved a node they share. Covered by
  `tests/test-plane-constrained-fit.mjs` (1- and 2-anchor solves against a
  brute-force search over the admissible normals).
- Group math: `triangulateAndReproject(instanceGroup, cameras, options)`
  (`options.method` = `'dlt'`|`'ba'`, `options.triangulateOnly`,
  `options.robustScale` (BA soft-L1 scale in px), `options.includedCameras`,
  `options.reprojErrorThreshold`; returns
  `.method`, `.meanError`/`.errors` distorted-space and
  `.meanErrorUndistorted`/`.errorsUndistorted` ideal-pinhole-space),
  `storeReprojectedInstances(group, triangulationResult, allCameras)`.
  **`storeReprojectedInstances` eagerly builds a full `Instance` (+ its own
  `occluded` array) per camera per group — a real memory cost project-wide**, so
  the bulk sweeps (`triangulateAllFrames`, `triangulateMultiFrameInstances`) do
  not call it (see "Neither Triangulate All path keeps derived state";
  `buildReprojH5` projects the stored 3D of a group with no reprojections).
  Single-frame paths (`triangulateCurrentFrame`, `reTriangulateGroup`) still do.
  `getOrComputeReprojectedInstance(group, camName)` — the read-side companion:
  the cached `reprojectedInstances` entry if present, else an equivalent
  `Instance` synthesized from `group.reprojections[camName]` (never cached onto
  the group). Every consumer goes through it instead of
  `group.getReprojectedInstance`: `import-export/file-io.js`'s three export
  sites, `pose/initialization.js`'s double-click-to-promote and
  `onClonePredictedGroup`, `ui/interaction.js`'s click hit-testing and
  `_convertToUserInstance`. `ui/rendering.js`'s `drawAllOverlays` has its own
  lazy-fill (computes and caches `reprojectedInstances`/`.reprojections`/
  `state.triangulationResults` for the viewed frame, for the last
  `DERIVED_FRAMES_KEEP` frames); `ui/overlays.js` reads
  `.reprojections[viewName]` directly (`drawReprojectedSkeleton`). Covered by
  `tests/test-reprojection-lifecycle.js`'s "getOrComputeReprojectedInstance" block.
  `reprojectIntoEveryCamera(reprojections, points3d, cameras)` — adds
  `reprojectPointsCamera(points3d, cam)` in place for every calibrated camera
  missing from `reprojections` (a group's reprojection must reach every camera,
  including one where it has no instance on that frame); 3D and errors are
  untouched. **No sweep calls it**: none stores reprojections, and the display
  fill reprojects the stored 3D into every camera (`reprojectStoredPoints3d`).
  It stays exported as the reference that `tests/test-reproject-stored-3d.mjs`
  and `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-bounded-derived-state.mjs` compare the derived
  state against. See also `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-reprojects-every-view.mjs`.
  **Two robustness features:** (1) views excluded in the Tracking Wizard's Camera
  Views panel (`isCameraTracked`, or `options.includedCameras` override) never
  contribute to the 3D solve, but are still reprojected INTO. (2)
  Reprojection-error threshold (Tracking Wizard `reprojErrorThreshold` px, opt-in /
  default 0 = off, or `options.reprojErrorThreshold`): excludes a
  **node-in-a-view** (one 2D keypoint) whose reprojection error exceeds it — never
  a whole view (the wizard's job) — the single worst per node per pass,
  re-triangulating between passes; a node left with <2 views under the threshold
  is nulled. Covered by `tests/test-triangulation-robust.js`.
- Lazy loading: class `LazyFrameLoader` (analysis `.h5`, worker-backed) +
  `shouldUseLazyH5(file)`; `shouldUseLazySlp(file)` + `LAZY_SLP_THRESHOLD` route
  large prediction `.slp` to the main-thread `SioLazyLoader`
  (`loading/sio-lazy-loader.js`). Shared consumers: `ensureLazyFrameData`,
  `buildLazyFrameGroupSync`, `batchLoadLazyFrames` (branches on `loader.isSync`
  for worker-free loaders), `loadAllLazyFrames`, `ensureLazyTrailWindow(frameIdx,
  trailLength)`, and `evictLazyFrames(anchorFrame)`
  — the app-state wrapper around `pose/lazy-residency.js`'s
  `evictLazyFrameGroups`. (Also exported: `lazyCamerasMissingFrom`, below.) A
  no-op until `session.frameGroups` exceeds `LAZY_RESIDENT_CAP`; then it protects
  windows around the on-screen frame and `anchorFrame`, keeps at least the
  node-trail length behind them, passes every object the InteractionManager
  holds (`_uiHeldObjects`: selection, Group-mode picks, an unlinked drag, the
  Edit Group target) and `state.triangulationResults`. Called after a NEW frame
  is hydrated by `ensureLazyFrameData`, `ensureLazyTrailWindow` and the playback
  loader (`ui/ui-wiring.js`), never from `batchLoadLazyFrames`. The loader's own
  caches are bounded separately (its 100-frame adapted-dict LRU and
  sleap-io.js's `frameCacheLimit`).
  `ensureLazyTrailWindow(frameIdx, trailLength)` hydrates the `trailLength`
  frames BEHIND `frameIdx` — the node-trail window, which a seek's own
  hydration (target + 30 ahead) does not reach. Called by `drawAllOverlays` and
  by the overlay-video export (preview and each exported frame, with the
  export's own trail length). Synchronous for `SioLazyLoader` (built before it
  returns, then one `evictLazyFrames`); for the worker loader it returns a
  Promise of the frames loaded and keeps at most one request in flight. Nearly
  free when the window is already resident (`tests/e2e/_bench-trail-window.mjs`).
  `LazyFrameLoader` spawns `loading/slp-import-worker.js` (resolved against
  `document.baseURI` so sub-path deployments work) for HDF5 reads.
  **`LazyFrameLoader`'s tracks are the union over its cameras, whichever worker
  answers first.** Each worker's `metadata` message goes through
  `_registerCamera`, which picks the skeleton of the first camera BY NAME and
  calls `_unifyTracks`: `trackNames` = the union of every camera's own names
  (`loading/track-union.js`; `_ownTrackNames` pads a list shorter than the
  data's `nTracks` with the worker's own `track_<i>` convention), plus a
  per-camera own→session map kept only where it is not the identity. A worker
  only knows its own file, so frames are re-indexed as they ARRIVE, in
  `onmessage` (`_remapFrameTracks` on `frameData` and `framesData`) — the one
  place `getFrame`, `prefetch` and `batchLoadLazyFrames` all pass through. A
  camera whose map moved has its dense occupancy grid re-keyed into the SPARSE
  form by `denseOccupancyToSparse` (a wider dense grid would cost nFrames × the
  whole union). Covered by `tests/test-lazy-track-union.js`.
  **A hydrated trackless instance is `trackIdx: null`, never `-1`.** Both lazy
  loaders hand over the store's trackless value, `-1`; the four hydration paths —
  `ensureLazyFrameData`, `hydrateLazyCameras`, `buildLazyFrameGroupSync`,
  `batchLoadLazyFrames` (both branches) — go through `lazyInstanceTrackIdx`
  (`>= 0` kept, anything else `null`), matching the eager paths
  (`resolveImportTrackIdx`) and every reader's `trackIdx == null` test
  (otherwise: the last track colour, a "Track -1" label, a lost identity). The
  STORE keeps `-1` — `appendStore`, `forEachInstanceRow` and
  `remapTracksFromIdentity` speak it — so the mapping happens exactly where a
  store row becomes an `Instance`. `frameIdentityMap` keys `null` and `-1` alike
  (`Session._fimKey`). Covered by `tests/e2e/lazy-trackless-null.mjs`.
  **A FrameGroup that exists is not necessarily complete.**
  A bare `if (session.frameGroups.has(frameIdx)) return;` is only sound when
  every camera is lazy-backed: in a part-eager, part-lazy session the eager
  cameras create the FrameGroup at load time. `ensureLazyFrameData` therefore
  asks which lazy cameras the existing group carries no data for
  (`lazyCamerasMissingFrom` — checks BOTH `fg.instances` and the unlinked pool,
  since the per-camera folder loader moves everything into the latter) and
  hydrates only those (`hydrateLazyCameras`). The new rows are staged in a
  throwaway FrameGroup so `finalizeLazyFrameGroup` runs on exactly the new
  cameras and cannot re-unlink instances an eager parse already placed. A camera
  hydrated concurrently is re-checked after the `await`, so a racing scrub cannot
  double-add. `lazyCamerasMissingFrom` is **exported** and unit-tested in
  `tests/test-lazy-camera-hydration.js` — it can fail in two opposite directions
  (every instance rendered twice, or a blank view). The folder loader no longer
  produces a mixed session at all (see `loading/session-loader.js`); this is the
  independent half, covered by `tests/e2e/percam-mixed-lazy-eager.mjs`.
  **`_rawInstIndex` tagging.** All three lazy-materialization sites
  (`ensureLazyFrameData`, `buildLazyFrameGroupSync`, and the worker-batch
  branch of `batchLoadLazyFrames`) tag every constructed `Instance` with
  `inst._rawInstIndex = ii` — its position within the frame's raw instance list,
  which equals its exact row offset in the lazy store's
  `[instance_id_start, instance_id_end)` range for that (camera, frame).
  `import-export/slp-streaming-write.js`'s `refFor` reads this on save instead of
  guessing the row via `trackIdx` (which attached the wrong animal's 2D). Test:
  `tests/e2e/save-multiinstance-ref-integrity.mjs`.
  **`finalizeLazyFrameGroup`** splits a freshly-(re)materialized lazy
  FrameGroup's raw instances into "already grouped" vs "unlinked", matching a raw
  instance to an existing group's member by `_rawInstIndex`. It checks
  `session.instanceGroups` directly for "does this frame already have groups?" —
  NOT `session._lazyReopened`, which only a reopen sets: a fresh Track All sweep
  evicts frames but never `instanceGroups`, and gating on the flag rendered each
  tracked animal TWICE. An ungrouped instance (`Session.unlinkGroup`) reappears
  unlinked on the next re-materialization. Test:
  `tests/e2e/lazy-frame-rematerialize-duplicate.mjs`.
  **Both `batchLoadLazyFrames` branches call `finalizeLazyFrameGroup`** — the
  `isSync` branch via `buildLazyFrameGroupSync`, the worker branch
  (`LazyFrameLoader`, analysis `.h5`) directly; without it a rebuilt frame dumps
  every raw instance into the unlinked pool and looks untracked. Test:
  `tests/e2e/batch-lazy-hydration-worker-loader.mjs`.
- Frame access: `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`,
  `frameHasGroupedUserInstances`, `updateTimelineForFrame`.
- Method preservation ("exported 3D == displayed 3D", see above):
  `resolveTriangulationMethod(group)`, `findEquivalentPriorGroup(priorGroups,
  group)`, `adoptPrior3d(group, prior, method)` (not `reprojections`,
  deliberately). The latter two exist for the regrouping sweeps, which rebuild
  `InstanceGroup` objects around unchanged `Instance`s.
- Orchestration: `triangulateMultiFrameInstances(start, end, onProgress, method)`
  -> `{ triangulated, totalGroups, errSum, errN, frames }` (keeps only the 3D;
  drops the derived state of each frame it re-solves),
  `dropAllDerivedTriangulationState(session)` (every group's reprojections and
  reprojected instances, and `state.triangulationResults`; both Triangulate All
  paths and Group by Track call it before they start),
  `reTriangulateGroup` (preserves the group's existing method via
  `resolveTriangulationMethod`; the auto-triangulation of a group created by
  Shift+G — does nothing without a real calibration, `sessionHasCalibration()`,
  so placeholder cameras never produce 3D that would be saved and exported),
  `triangulateCurrentFrame(method)`, `triangulateAllFrames(method)`
  (`method` defaults to `'dlt'`), `sessionHasCalibration`,
  `showCalibrationRequiredPopup`,
  `ensureGroupsFromIdentities(session, frameIdx)` — auto-creates a frame's
  InstanceGroups from its per-frame identity assignments (>=2-camera buckets;
  explicit-none stays unlinked) when none exist yet. Both
  `triangulateCurrentFrame` and `triangulateAllFrames` call it. (Track All itself
  writes InstanceGroups via `commitTrackedFrame`; the function's source comment
  still says Track All "does NOT group", which is stale.)
  `triangulateAllFrames` sweeps every frame (not just pre-grouped ones). Its
  **unlinked** rows are bucketed with
  `session.getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance`, not the track-keyed resolver: a
  trackless ungrouped instance carries its identity on the instance, not in the
  trackIdx-keyed map, so asking by track drops exactly the instances an ungroup
  produced. `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll` (`ui/export-modals.js`)
  duplicates this bucketing and must stay in step.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./pose-data.js` — `mat3x3Multiply`, `Camera`, `FrameGroup`, `Instance`,
  `UnlinkedInstance`, `InstanceGroup`, the flat-`points3d` helpers
  (`makePoints3d`, `points3dNodeCount`, `hasPoint3d`, `getPoint3d`,
  `readPoint3d`, `setPoint3d`, `clearPoint3d`, `someValidPoint3d`,
  `countPoints3d`) and `pooledPoints3d` (`_applyGroupStep` stores every
  Triangulate All result in the slab pool).
- `./plane-fit.js` — `jacobiEigen`, `fitPlaneToPoints3d` (re-exported).
- `../ui/app-state.js` — `state`, `timeline`, `viewport3d`, `interactionManager`
  (the objects `evictLazyFrames` must not drop out from under the UI).
- `./lazy-residency.js` — `evictLazyFrameGroups`, `holdLazyResidency`,
  `releaseLazyResidency`, `releaseFrameMembers2d`, `LAZY_RESIDENT_CAP`,
  `LAZY_KEEP_BEHIND`,
  `dropDerivedReprojections` (Triangulate Range drops each re-solved frame's
  derived state).
- `../ui/rendering.js` — `setReprojErrorVisible`, `showReprojectionsOnly`,
  `REPROJ_ONLY_NOTE` (Triangulate All ends Reproj-only), `drawAllOverlays`.
- `../ui/info-panel.js` — `updateTriangulationBadge`.
- `../ui/settings.js` — `isCameraTracked`, `getTrackingThreshold`,
  `getDefaultTriangulationMethod` (the fallback in `resolveTriangulationMethod`).
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `markDirty`, `setStatus`,
  `showLoading`, `hideLoading`.
- `../ui/loading-overlay.js` — `showLoadingProgress`, `createProgressPacer`,
  `yieldToPaint` (Triangulate All's eager loop and `sweepLazyFrameWindows`), and
  `setLoadingCpu`: both Triangulate All paths hand the overlay their solver's
  `cpuBusyMs()` right after creating it (`showSolverCpu`), so the overlay shows a
  CPU line and a CPU-only load graph whose tooltip names the workers, or
  "solving on the main thread" when inline. `hideLoading` stops it.
- `./triangulation-core.js` — the pure math (re-exported) and
  `setTriangulationSettingsHooks`.
- `./triangulation-pool.js` — `createGroupSolver` (Triangulate All's parallel solve).
- `./run-state.js` — `noteTriangulateRun` (at the end of `triangulateAllFrames`,
  both paths, `triangulateCurrentFrame` and `triangulateMultiFrameInstances`)
  and `carryTriangulateMark` (`reTriangulateGroup`): the blue Triangulate
  button.
- `../loading/track-union.js` — `unionTrackNames`, `remapTrackIdx`,
  `isIdentityRemap` (`LazyFrameLoader`'s track union).
- `./initialization.js` — `update3DViewport` (circular).

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js`, `pose/initialization.js`,
`import-export/file-io.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `loading/session-loader.js`, `ui/rendering.js`,
`ui/info-panel.js`, `ui/interaction.js`, `ui/identity-assignment.js`,
`ui/export-modals.js`, `ui/overlay-export-modal.js`, `ui/plane-angle.js`,
`ui/plane-definition.js`, `ui/plane-nodes-panel.js`, `ui/sessions-panes.js`,
`ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**User-facing features.** "Triangulate" key (`T`), Edit menu Triangulate
Frame / All / Multi-Frame, reprojection-error visualization, lazy SLP
loading, "Triangulation needed" badge.

### pose/lazy-residency.js

**Purpose.** Keep a LAZY project's resident frames bounded during playback,
without ever dropping a frame that holds anything the store cannot rebuild. On a
lazy project `session.frameGroups` is meant to be a window; without eviction
every played frame stays resident and the heap climbs toward the renderer's
~4.2 GB limit, and after a Triangulate All the draw path also caches
reprojections for every drawn frame (`fillLazyReprojections`).

**Key exports.** `evictLazyFrameGroups(session, { anchors, ahead, behind, cap,
refs, triangulationResults })` -> `{ resident, evicted, protected, blocked,
membersReleased }` or null (no lazy loader, at/under `cap`, or held);
`frameEvictionBlocker(session, frameIdx, fg, refs)` -> a reason string or null;
`releaseFrameMembers2d(session, frameIdx, { refs })` -> members released;
`hydrateFrameMembers2d(session, frameIdx)` -> members hydrated;
`member2dReleaseBlocker(member, desc, numNodes, refs)` -> a reason or null;
`dropDerivedReprojections(session, frameIdx, triangulationResults)` — clear one
frame's groups' reprojections / reprojected instances and its results entry
(also used by `ui/rendering.js`'s cap on derived frames and by Triangulate
Range, `triangulateMultiFrameInstances`, for each frame it re-solves);
`dropGroupDerivedReprojections(group, frameIdx, triangulationResults)` — the same
for ONE group, leaving the frame's other groups and their results entries alone
(moving a view between sessions, the points3d `.h5` import); `holdLazyResidency` /
`releaseLazyResidency` / `isLazyResidencyHeld` (nestable); `lastLazyEvictionPass()`
(diagnostics); `LAZY_PLAYBACK_AHEAD` (600, the playback loader's lookahead),
`LAZY_KEEP_BEHIND` (512, > the 500-frame maximum node trail),
`LAZY_RESIDENT_CAP` (1536), `LAZY_PREFETCH_MARGIN` (32, covers
`ensureLazyFrameData`'s 30-frame scrub prefetch).

**Contract — a frame is dropped only if ALL hold:**
- It is outside every protected window `[anchor - behind, anchor + ahead]`
  (default `ahead` = `LAZY_PLAYBACK_AHEAD + LAZY_PREFETCH_MARGIN`).
- No hold is active (the windowed sweeps hold for their whole run).
- **Re-hydration would rebuild exactly what is resident** (`frameEvictionBlocker`
  returns null). Re-hydration makes one Instance per store row tagged
  `_rawInstIndex`, re-seats this frame's `instanceGroups` members by
  `_rawInstIndex` (`finalizeLazyFrameGroup`) and unlinks the rest. So: every
  camera with data must be backed by the loader (`'camera'`); per camera the
  resident instances must be a bijection onto the store rows (`'count'`, `'row'`);
  linked instances must be exactly the members re-seated there and unlinked ones
  must not be (`'grouping'`); no instance may be `'user'` (the streaming save
  reads its edit overlay from RESIDENT frames — `buildSessionRefGraph`), modified,
  backed-up mid-edit or nulled (`'modified'`), resized by a skeleton edit
  (`'skeleton'`), or held by the UI (`'ui'`); and each unlinked instance must
  still match its row — track (`'track'`), type (`'type'`), and no #201 identity
  stamped on the instance (`'identity'`, not persisted). Group members' own fields
  are not compared: they survive in `instanceGroups` (never evicted) and come back
  as the SAME objects. Points are not compared — every 2D edit path promotes to
  `user`, sets `modified` or `nulledNodes`. A loader without
  `describeStoreFrame` (the worker-backed `LazyFrameLoader`) never evicts.
- Dropping a frame also drops its DERIVED reprojection caches
  (`group.reprojections`, `group.reprojectedInstances`, its
  `triangulationResults` entry) — the state `sweepTriangulateAllFrames` leaves
  every frame in; `fillLazyReprojections` re-derives them on the next draw.

**Group members' 2D goes back to the store too.** `instanceGroups` is never
evicted, but a member of a frame that is NOT resident need not hold its 2D — a
private `Float64Array` per member after Track All is GBs of heap and millions of
ArrayBuffers for every full GC to sweep. `releaseFrameMembers2d` points each
releasable member at the shared `lazyPlaceholderXY` and sets `_lazy2d` — exactly
a reopened project's lightweight member — and `finalizeLazyFrameGroup` re-adopts
the row's 2D the next time the frame is hydrated. Rules:
- **Only for a NON-resident frame** (its members are what is on screen), never
  for a group or member the UI holds, and only for a member re-hydration would
  give back exactly (`member2dReleaseBlocker`): an untouched prediction — not
  `'user'`, `'modified'`/mid-edit/nulled, no `'occlusion'` set (hydration brings
  occlusion back clear), no `'skeleton'` change — whose store `'row'` exists and
  is a prediction. Its other fields (track, type, score, identity) stay on the
  member object; only `_xy` is given back.
- **Re-adoption keys on the member's CURRENT `_rawInstIndex`**, never a cached
  row or position — a store compaction (Custom Delete, interactive deletes)
  renumbers released members too, and must keep pointing each at its own row.
- **Where it runs:** wherever a frame stops being resident — the windowed
  sweeps' window release and `evictLazyFrameGroups`. A reader that needs member
  2D of a frame it does not hydrate brackets the read with
  `hydrateFrameMembers2d` / `releaseFrameMembers2d` (the image ID-switch check's
  `frameCropGeometry`, `ui/image-embedder.js`); `hydrateFrameMembers2d` builds
  each row exactly as `buildLazyFrameGroupSync` + `finalizeLazyFrameGroup` do
  and does NOT make the frame resident.

**Imports from project modules.** `./pose-data.js` (`Instance`,
`lazyPlaceholderXY`) — still DOM-free, so the Node test can drive it.

**Imported by.** `pose/triangulation.js` (`evictLazyFrames`,
`sweepLazyFrameWindows`), `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`LAZY_PLAYBACK_AHEAD`),
`ui/image-embedder.js` and `ui/identity-gallery.js` (`hydrateFrameMembers2d`,
`releaseFrameMembers2d`), `ui/sessions-panes.js` (those two plus
`dropGroupDerivedReprojections`), `ui/rendering.js` (`dropDerivedReprojections`),
`import-export/slp-import.js` (`dropGroupDerivedReprojections`).

**Coverage.** `tests/test-lazy-residency.mjs` (every blocker against an untouched
twin, windows, cap, holds, the reprojection drop, a simulated playback, and the
constants against the code they protect); `tests/e2e/lazy-playback-eviction.mjs`
(`describeStoreFrame` vs the real sleap-io.js materializer on every row, and
evict -> re-hydrate through the real path rebuilding every frame identically);
`tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs` cycle 7 (edits, the real playback loader
played far past the cap, save, reopen — a member's edit must survive the save).
