### pose/triangulation-core.js

**Purpose.** The pure triangulation math, split out of `pose/triangulation.js`
so a Web Worker can load it: matrix helpers (`matMul`, `matTranspose`,
`solveSmallestEigenvector4x4`, `svd3x4`), DLT
(`triangulatePointDLT`, `triangulatePoints`, and `DltViewSystem` for solving
one keypoint again on subsets of its views), the "Refined" point stage
(`triangulatePointBA`, `triangulatePointsBA`, `BA_ROBUST_SCALE_PX`),
reprojection (`reprojectPoint(s)`, `reprojectPointCamera(s)`, `cameraDepth` —
the homogeneous `w`, POSITIVE in front of the camera, which any caller
reprojecting into a camera it did not choose must gate on), errors
(`computeReprojectionError(s)`, `computeMeanReprojectionError`), `invert3x3`,
the full per-group pipeline `triangulateAndReproject`, its reprojection half
on already-solved 3D (`reprojectStoredPoints3d`), `triangulationSummary`, and the cross-view
tracker's geometry — `hungarianAlgorithm`, `cameraCenter`, `backProjectToRay`,
`backProjectToRays` (with their `_pGeometry` / `_pseudoInverse` WeakMap memo),
`pointToRayDistance`, `pointsToRayDistances`, `computeFundamentalMatrix`,
`epipolarError`, `epipolarErrorMatrix` — here so `pose/tracker-worker.js` can
load the tracker.

**Key exports.** All of the above, plus `setTriangulationSettingsHooks({
isCameraTracked, getTrackingThreshold })` — the only app state the pipeline
reads (unset = every camera included, no threshold; the main thread installs
them, a worker gets resolved values via `options.includedCameras` /
`options.reprojErrorThreshold`) — and `__triangulationKernelsForTest`.

**`reprojectStoredPoints3d(group, cameras, options?)`** — steps 3-5 of
`triangulateAndReproject` (reproject, per-camera errors in native and
undistorted pixels, both means) run on `group.points3d` instead of a fresh
solve; same result shape, `points3d` IS the group's array, `method` = the
group's `triangulationMethod`. Errors are measured in the cameras the group
has an instance in (`prep.groupCameras`, as the bulk solves do); reprojections
cover every camera. The two functions share `_collectObservations` /
`_reprojectAndScore`, so for a group whose 3D is that solve's output the
numbers are identical (Object.is) — except, with a reprojection-error
threshold, the UNDISTORTED errors: the solve leaves out the observations it
dropped, which the stored 3D does not record. ~20x cheaper than re-solving
Refined. Used by `ui/rendering.js`'s reprojection fill.
**`triangulationSummary(result)`** — `{points3d, meanError,
meanErrorUndistorted, method}`, what a pool reply carries under `summaryOnly`.
Covered by `tests/test-reproject-stored-3d.mjs` (incl. the threshold negative
control).

**Allocation-free hot paths, bit-identical by construction — except the DLT's
null-vector solve.**
- DLT matrix: `dltNormalMatrixFlat` builds M = AᵀA with the exact operations of
  row arrays -> `matTranspose` -> `matMul` (same A entries, same k-ordered sums
  from 0) on preallocated typed arrays; summing only the upper triangle and
  mirroring is exact (IEEE 754 multiplication commutes).
- DLT solve: `dltHomogeneousFlat` finds M's null vector with
  `smallestEigvec4Inverse` — **inverse iteration** (Cholesky of `M + δI`,
  `δ = 1e-15·trace`, iterated until no entry moves by more than `INVERSE_TOL`
  1e-15). This is the one deliberate break from bit-identity: the Jacobi kernel
  it replaced, `smallestEigvec4Flat` (still bit-identical to `jacobiEigen` ->
  smallest-|λ| pick), was 23% of a whole Track All; inverse iteration is ~5x
  faster per solve. After `INVERSE_MAX_ITER` (50) steps, or for a non-finite M
  or one that does not factor, it falls back to `smallestEigvec4Flat`. It must
  CONVERGE rather than run a fixed number of steps: the start vector does not
  rotate with the world, so an unconverged answer depends on the frame, and the
  frame-invariance tests catch that (a 1e-5 tolerance fails two of them).
  Measured with `tests/e2e/_diag-trackall-ab.mjs` on seven real recordings:
  identical identity groups on every frame, 3D within 8.6e-10 mm.
- Subset DLT: `DltViewSystem` (`prepare(observations, Ps)`, `solve(use, out)`)
  forms one keypoint's per-view DLT rows (`dltRowsInto`, shared with
  `dltNormalMatrixFlat`) and their upper-triangle products once, in the frame of
  the same `Ps` array; `solve` adds the used views' products in row order, so it
  is bit-identical to `triangulatePointDLT` with the other views nulled.
  (Subtracting a view from the full M would not be.) Used by the tracker's
  `checkedPoints3d`. `smallestEigvec4Inverse`'s Cholesky and triangular solves
  are unrolled into locals in the same operation order — the same bits, pinned
  against a verbatim copy of the loop form — which speeds up every DLT solve.
- Refinement: `triangulatePointBA` projects through
  `_projectAndJacobian(Camera)Into`, which write into one scratch object with
  the same expressions as `projectAndJacobian`, `distortJacobian` and
  `Camera.distortPoint` (including their different r²·r² vs r⁴ association).
Pinned by `tests/test-triangulation-kernels.mjs` (`Object.is` per value for the
bit-identical parts; for the inverse iteration: agreement with Jacobi,
Rayleigh quotient no worse, rotation invariance, the fallbacks). **Any edit to
these kernels must keep operation order,** or results drift in the last bits
and tracker decisions can flip — and a change to the solve itself needs a
before/after Track All on real recordings (`tests/e2e/_diag-trackall-ab.mjs`),
since a tiny 3D difference is no proof that no association flips.

**`triangulatePointDLT` SOLVES IN A CAMERA-DERIVED FRAME, and that is
load-bearing.** DLT minimizes an ALGEBRAIC error, and `‖x‖ = 1` on a
HOMOGENEOUS 4-vector weights `(X,Y,Z)` against `W`, so in the raw calibration
frame the answer depends on WHERE THE ORIGIN IS — which
`Set as New Calibration` moves by ~1.2 m while promising to change no geometry
(on a real cage session, 21% of frames grouped differently by Track All).
The system is built in `dltNormalizingFrame` (centroid of the camera centres,
unit = their mean distance from it, cached per `projectionMatrices` array),
which moves with the cameras under a rigid change of world, so the null vector
maps exactly. Rules:
- **The frame must depend on the cameras ONLY.** Deriving it from the
  observations, or from a first-pass answer, makes it depend on the thing being
  solved for and the invariance argument collapses.
- **Degenerate projection matrices fall back to the raw frame** — a worse
  answer beats no answer.
- **A noiseless fixture cannot pin this**: when the rays meet exactly every
  re-weighting finds the same null vector, so
  `tests/e2e/triangulation-frame-invariant.mjs` injects noise deliberately.
- `dltHomogeneousFlat` takes the frame as four optional trailing arguments
  (`s, cx, cy, cz`); omitted is the identity frame, which reproduces the raw
  rows exactly (×1 and +0 are exact in IEEE 754), so the kernel bit-identity
  above is unaffected.

**Imports from project modules.** `./pose-data.js` (point3d helpers, and `mat3x3Multiply` for `computeFundamentalMatrix`) and
`./plane-fit.js` for `jacobiEigen`, which is re-exported from here. That
function has ONE home in `plane-fit.js`: `pose/plane-data.js` must not import
`pose/triangulation.js` (and the whole UI with it), and `plane-fit.js` depends
only on `./pose-data.js`, so a worker can still load this module.

**Imported by.** `pose/triangulation.js` (re-exports), `pose/triangulation-pool.js`,
`pose/triangulation-worker.js`, `pose/cross-view-tracker.js` (and so
`pose/tracker-worker.js`).

---

### pose/triangulation-pool.js

**Purpose.** Run `triangulateAndReproject` for many instance groups on a pool
of Web Workers — the Triangulate All paths' parallel solve. Every group is
independent (cameras fixed).

**Key exports.** `createGroupSolver(cameras, { method, triangulateOnly,
summaryOnly, expectedGroups })` -> `{ submit(group, groupCameras, cb), mark(cb), throttle(),
finish(), cancel(), cpuBusyMs(), parallel, workers }`; `MIN_GROUPS_FOR_WORKERS` (2000).
`summaryOnly`: each result is `triangulationSummary(result)` (3D + mean errors),
reduced inline and in the worker before the reply, so the per-camera arrays
(~52 KB per group at 17 cameras x 34 nodes) are never cloned back only to be
thrown away.

**Contract.**
- **Callbacks run in submission order** (jobs and `mark`s interleaved as
  submitted), so every side effect on app state happens in the old inline
  loop's order. `throttle()` bounds in-flight work (~2 batches per worker).
- A job's inputs — each camera's `_xy` copy and nulled nodes, plus the two
  settings resolved ONCE per run (the overlay blocks the UI) — are captured at
  `submit`, so a lazy window may be released while its solves are in flight.
- Cameras are shipped with the main thread's cached `R` / `Rt` / `P`, so the
  worker never re-derives a matrix. Results are bit-identical to inline.
- Inline fallback (synchronous at `submit`, same callbacks): no `Worker`, small
  runs (`expectedGroups` < 2000, where start-up dominates), or
  `window.LUCID_TRIANGULATION_WORKERS = 0`. Setting it to N > 0 forces an
  N-worker pool regardless of size (tests). A batch a worker fails on — or a
  worker that dies — is re-solved inline from the captured job.
- Pool size `min(hardwareConcurrency - 1, 16)`; workers persist between runs
  and are terminated after 30 s idle. One solver owns the pool at a time (a
  concurrent second run solves inline). Every call site passes `method:
  method` explicitly (`tests/test-triangulation-method-propagation.mjs`).
- **`cpuBusyMs()`** — the run's CPU time so far, thread-ms summed over threads,
  for the loading overlay's CPU line (`createBusyClock`, `ui/load-graph.js`, one
  per solver). One lane per worker slot (from `postMessage` to its reply), and
  `'main'`, open from `createGroupSolver` to `finish` / `cancel` EXCEPT while
  the main thread waits on the workers (`waitOn` in `throttle` / `finish`); a
  reply handled during such a wait reopens `'main'` (nested, so it is not
  counted twice). Inline, `'main'` never closes: exactly one core.

**Imports from project modules.** `./triangulation-core.js`
(`triangulateAndReproject`, `triangulationSummary`), `./pose-data.js` (`Instance`), `../ui/settings.js`
(`isCameraTracked`, `getTrackingThreshold`, read once per run in
`createGroupSolver`), `../ui/load-graph.js` (`createBusyClock`). The pool runs on
the main thread only — the worker loads `./triangulation-core.js`, never this
module — so these ui/ imports do not reach the worker.

**Imported by.** `pose/triangulation.js` (`triangulateAllFrames`,
`sweepTriangulateAllFrames`), `ui/export-modals.js`
(`groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll`).

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-worker-pool.mjs` (every Triangulate
All route, inline vs a forced 4-worker pool, must hash identically) and
`tests/e2e/triangulate-all-cpu-load.mjs` (`cpuBusyMs()`).

---

### pose/triangulation-worker.js

**Purpose.** Module worker for `pose/triangulation-pool.js`: rebuilds each job's
group (real `Instance`s from flat `_xy` + nulled nodes) and cameras (with the
main thread's cached matrices installed) and runs the same
`triangulateAndReproject` from `./triangulation-core.js`. Messages: in
`{type:'cameras'}`, `{type:'solve', id, jobs}`; out `{type:'solved', id,
results}` (points3d buffers transferred; `triangulationSummary(result)` per job
whose options say `summaryOnly`) or `{type:'error', id, message}`.

**Imports from project modules.** `./pose-data.js` (`Camera`, `Instance`),
`./triangulation-core.js` (`triangulateAndReproject`, `triangulationSummary`).

**Imported by.** Spawned by `pose/triangulation-pool.js` via
`new Worker(new URL('pose/triangulation-worker.js', document.baseURI), {type:'module'})`.

---

### pose/id-switch-check.js

**Purpose.** The analysis behind Tracks ▸ **Check ID Switches (Body Size)…**,
**(Images)…** and **(Coat Brightness)…**, which also run automatically after Track
All / Track Frame Range (`ui/id-switch-modal.js`). Flags contacts between two
tracked identities where the labels may have changed hands — a possible identity
switch. Body size (3D bone lengths, cheap, no video) and appearance (embeddings of
masked, pose-aligned crops, supplied by the caller — `ui/image-embedder.js` in the
app) go through ONE pipeline (`scoreContacts`) that asks whether each animal looks
like ITSELF across the contact, using no identity labels across it. Coat brightness
(the grey level at each animal's body keypoints — `ui/brightness-sampler.js`, a
4-number vector; single camera) keeps the method both used before #309 / #310
(`checkClassifierSwitches`): whether the animals leaving an encounter match the
identities they now carry, by a classifier learned from the tracker's labels, plus
candidate moments tested on both sides — it was calibrated on that. Pure — no DOM,
no app state — so all run headlessly (the image and brightness checks with any
vector provider).

**Key exports.** `isTailNode(name)` (a name starting with "tail", or `t` and a number —
`t0`, `t1`, `t2`, as some SLEAP skeletons name the tail — in any case; tail nodes are
left out of an animal's body: its centroid here, its crop and coat brightness in
`ui/image-embedder.js` `skeletonIndex`); `findNode(names, name)` (a node's index matched regardless of
case: an exact match wins, then the first case-insensitive one, else -1 — the size
bones here and Nose / TTI in `ui/image-embedder.js` `skeletonIndex`, since SLEAP
skeletons name nodes freely: `nose`, `tti`, …); `checkSizeSwitches(session, opts)` and
`checkImageSwitches(session, opts)` (async) -> `{ok, flags, changes ([]),
encounters (the scored contacts), identities, sampledFrames, closeDistance,
threshold, fps, step, sampleHz, cue}` (+ `bones` for size; + `imageHz`, `crops`,
`cameras`, `samples: {embedded, of, rounds}` for images) or `{ok:false, reason}`;
`checkBrightnessSwitches(session, opts)` (async) -> the classifier's `{ok, flags,
changes, encounters, moments, identities, sampledFrames, closeDistance, threshold,
continueBelow, skipEmpty, clearestEndLeads, fps, step, sampleHz, cue: 'brightness',
imageHz, crops, cameras}`; the
pipeline's parts — `buildGrid(session, o, needSize)`, `contactEpisodes(grid,
gapSeconds)`, `learnIdentityMetric(X, n, D, stretch, sample, o, weights?)`,
`contactChangeScores(grid, feat, o, every)`, `markContactChanges(scored, o)`
(the change-point step, returning the change points; exported so a threshold can
be re-applied to the same scores), `symmetricEigen(A, n)` (cyclic Jacobi) and
`fitPCA(X, n, D, k, {weights?})` (randomized subspace iteration); which image
samples to embed — `contactReads(grid, has, o, every)` (the samples the scoring
asks about, given which have features), `planImageSamples(grid, o, every, st)`,
`MAX_PLAN_ROUNDS` (4), `MAX_BACKGROUND_GAP_SECONDS` (10); the classifier's (coat
brightness) — `markChangePoints(scored, o)` (its change-point step, exported
likewise), `momentChangePoints(moments, scored, changes, o)` (merges tested
candidate moments into those change points; exported for the tests) and
`fitSoftmax(X, y, n, D, K, opts)` (L2 multinomial logistic regression, Adam);
`KEYFRAME_GAP_TOLERANCE` (1.1);
`planKeyframeSamples(frames, keyframes, hasFrame)` -> `{decode, snapped, spacing,
keyframeGap, maxShift}` (which frame each image sample decodes in one camera — see
"Keyframe sampling" under `ui/image-embedder.js`); `SIZE_BONES`;
`SIZE_CHECK_DEFAULTS` (`fps` REQUIRED, `sampleHz` 15, `minTrackedSeconds` 60,
`sepFactor` 0.65, `sepDistance` null (overrides the close distance),
`episodeGapSeconds` 3, `minApartSeconds` 3, `windowSeconds` 10,
`maxSpanSeconds` 60, `followSeconds` 5, `metricPcaDims` 64, `metricDims` 16,
`metricReg` 0.1, `signal`; `threshold` -1, `maxTrainRows` 200000);
`IMAGE_CHECK_DEFAULTS` (the same shared ones; `threshold` -0.8, `maxTrainRows`
20000, `imageHz` 2, `backgroundHz` 0.1, `getEmbeddings` REQUIRED: `async (frame,
items[{k, group}]) -> per item [{camera, vector}]`, STARTED in increasing frame order
with up to `inFlight` (default 2; the image embedder asks for 8) in flight; optional
`prepareFrames(frames, items, gridFrames)` (awaited once per round with the sorted
frames it will ask for next and the items at each — so a provider can skip what it
has cached — and every image sample's frame, a superset), `keyframes()` (per camera,
its keyframe frame indices: where decoding is cheap), `croppable(frames, items,
gridFrames)` (per frame, per item: will it get a crop? from the keypoints, before
decoding) and `releaseFrames()` (always called at the end) let the provider stream
its video).
`REFERENCE_HZ` (15); `CLASSIFIER_CHECK_DEFAULTS` (the size check's defaults before
#310: `fps` REQUIRED, `sampleHz` 15, `folds` 5, `gapSeconds` 10, `syncSeconds` 1,
`threshold` -50, `continueBelow` 0, `followSeconds` 60, `skipEmpty` false,
`clearestEndLeads` false, `minTrackedSeconds` 60, `sepFactor` 0.65, `signal`,
`moments` null, `momentSeconds` 15, `momentThreshold` -200, ...);
`BRIGHTNESS_CHECK_DEFAULTS` (those + `imageHz` 4, `threshold` -800, `pcaDims` 8,
`maxTrainRows` 6000, `iterations` 200 and the provider options — see "Coat
brightness keeps the classifier path" below).

**The grid.** Every round(fps / `sampleHz`)-th frame is a grid sample: per
identity its InstanceGroup, body centroid and bone lengths (the `SIZE_BONES` the
skeleton has, found by name regardless of case and reported in `bones` as the
skeleton names them). "Close" = body
centroids within `sepFactor` x the median body extent (unit-free). Tracklet = a
run where an identity is apart from every other.

**The method.** Features per (sample, identity) come in VIEWS: the image check
embeds crops every round(sampleHz / imageHz)-th grid sample, one view per
camera; the size check's one view is the 16 bone lengths of every grid sample,
each standardised by its spread over the session. Per view the features are
centred — embeddings also scaled to unit length (an embedding's direction is
what it encodes; a bone-length vector's size is the signal) — and projected
into a metric learned WITHOUT identity labels (`learnIdentityMetric`): an LDA
whose "same animal" pairs are features of one identity within one tracklet and
whose "different animals" pairs are two identities in the same frame — both
true however wrong the tracking is. Contacts are episodes (`contactEpisodes`:
the two identities in one connected component of the close graph, so a swap
through a third animal counts; runs < `episodeGapSeconds` apart merged), and
episodes separated by less than `minApartSeconds` of usable samples of either
animal are merged too, so a swap inside a long huddle is judged from the
stretches either side of it. Each episode is scored (`contactChangeScores`) per
view from the mean feature of each animal just before (aB, bB) and just after
(aA, bA) it — up to `windowSeconds` a side, within `maxSpanSeconds`, never past
the pair's neighbouring episode, a sample being usable when the animal is not
in contact with its partner (it may touch a third: a crop masks others out):
S = cos(aB,aA) + cos(bB,bA) − cos(aB,bA) − cos(bB,aA), mean over views seeing
both animals on both sides (episodes with none are dropped). Below `threshold`
the labels changed hands there (`markContactChanges`): taking the session's
start as right, a pair's 1st, 3rd, … flagged contact is an `onset` whose
`switchBackAt` is the next one (or null: to the end), its 2nd, 4th, … the `end`
of that stretch (`switchedAt`), so fixing either row swaps the same stretch;
all are in `flags` (`changes` is []), none is a repeat (`continues` is always
false), and a change point of a pair sharing one identity within
`followSeconds` of another is its `followOf` (a three-way relabel). A score is
a difference of cosines between mean features, so no sample rate scales it
(25-120 fps tested). `opts.signal` cancels (AbortError).

**Why not a classifier on the tracker's labels** (the earlier design): it learns
whichever labelling covers more of the session, and when a pair swaps several
times none does — on a real 3-mouse recording it missed the real swap and
raised 17 false change points. **Why the metric:** raw DINOv2 cosine separated
real changes from clean contacts at AUC 0.68 on five white mice; the metric,
0.78. **Why cosine, not distance, for size:** the distance-based alternative
(the dot product of the two animals' difference before and after) scored worse
on the real recordings, its scale swinging per recording. **Window means are
renormalised** in both checks: keeping their length silences noise-only dips on
animals of IDENTICAL size, but made size slightly worse and gained the image
check nothing out of sample.

**Crop sampling.** **Views per animal** (`imageCheckMaxViews`, default 3): the 3
largest views match all 8; 2 starts to cost. **Sampling below 2/s does not
work** — the tracklets after an encounter are short, so they need dense
samples; cheaper decoding comes from keyframe sampling instead (each sample
moves to its nearest keyframe, <= 0.25 s away — `planKeyframeSamples`), which
does not change the result (snapped vs unsnapped scores correlate 0.98).
Harness: `tests/e2e/_diag-image-keyframe-snap.mjs`.

**Which samples are embedded** (2026-10-22). Contact episodes come from the 3D
alone, so before decoding anything the check knows which image samples the
scoring will read: `contactReads` runs `contactChangeScores` on stand-in features
and records every sample it asks about — so the plan cannot drift from the
scoring. `planImageSamples` embeds those (up to `windowSeconds` either side of
each episode, at `imageHz`, and the episode-merge test's gap samples), plus a
BACKGROUND for the metric: one sample per block of round(imageHz / `backgroundHz`)
image samples that the scoring does not all read — the one cheapest to decode
(fewest frames past its keyframe and what the planned samples already decode,
summed over cameras; `keyframes()`), else the first. With keyframes sparser than
the block, a block spans a keyframe gap + a sample (at most
`MAX_BACKGROUND_GAP_SECONDS`), so it holds a sample just after a keyframe. Whole
samples are embedded (every identity in the frame), so "different animals in one
frame" pairs remain. A crop that never comes (no croppable view) makes the
scoring read further; `croppable()` tells the planner up front, and otherwise —
or when a decode fails — the check plans again after each pass until nothing is
left (at most `MAX_PLAN_ROUNDS`; the last embeds everything left). Then the
scoring reads exactly the samples it would read with every sample embedded
(`tests/test-id-switch-check.mjs` pins this, with a negative control). Only the
metric differs: it is learned from fewer crops, so `backgroundWeights` weights
each background sample by the unread samples of its block it stands for
(post-stratified; samples the scoring reads count once) — centring mean, PCA and
both LDA scatters. Weights of 1 are bit-identical to the unweighted fit, and
`backgroundHz` >= `imageHz` embeds every sample with no weights: the old check,
bit for bit. A later pass's rows are sorted back into sample order before the fit.
**Calibration (all 22 proofread SLAP sessions, CD1FSCR3_CSI, 05mice_flippers;
offline replay of each recording's full run, bit-exact against the browser).**
Without the weights the metric over-weights the windows: 144215 gained a false
row at every rate. Weighted, with keyframe placement, at `backgroundHz` 0.1, 0.12
and 0.15 every flagged contact on all 24 recordings is the one the full run flags
(caught/false per group unchanged: original six 4/7, 0 false, per-contact AUC
0.913 -> 0.914; held-out 16 11/12, 0 false, AUC 0.985 -> 0.981; CD1 its 4 real
relabels, 0 false). Scores move by a median 0.02-0.24 per session (max 0.75: a
contact at +1.8 moving to +1.0). Flags change only on contacts within 0.05 of -0.8 in the full run
(05mice 26:27 at -0.82, 190807 9:21 at -0.80, 144215 1:47 at -0.75), at 0.2,
0.08, 0.05 and 0. Crops saved: 28-73% on two-mouse sessions, 8-10% on the 4-mouse
ones, 1% on five huddling mice (their windows cover nearly everything). Frames
decoded (keyframe every 250): 28-82% of the full run on two-mouse sessions; a
background every 2 s placed without keyframes still decoded ~95%. **First run**
(M2 Pro, WebGPU fp16, full and planned runs alternated): two-mouse SLAP 140918 77-79 s
-> 24 s, 131531 82-141 s -> 55-72 s (Drive-mounted videos, decode-bound: reads vary);
CD1 (3 mice) 140-155 s -> 75-88 s; 05mice 403-547 s -> 379-401 s (model-bound, 1%
fewer crops). A re-run after re-tracking still takes 95-99% of its crops from the
cache (`ui/image-embedder.js`), with the same result as a cache-off run.

**Calibration (8 real recordings, 1 h 45 min; a row is right when both its
identities change animals within its contact ± 3 s).** Images at -0.8: 10 of 12
relabels caught, 2 false rows; robust to every knob swept (`metricDims`,
`metricReg`, `windowSeconds`, `minApartSeconds`, `episodeGapSeconds`,
`metricPcaDims`) except `metricDims` 8 and `minApartSeconds` 0 (the
evidence-driven merge is what scores a swap inside a huddle). Size at -1: 6 of
12 caught, 7 false (all on five white mice); `metricDims` 2/4 are worse.
Bone lengths cannot tell apart animals of one size, but catch swaps images
miss; rows both checks flag were real 5 of 5. **Out of sample** (16 proofread
sessions not used to choose anything): images 9 of 11, 0 false (per-contact
AUC 1.000); size 2 of 11, 0 false. A swap inside a pair's first contact at the
session start cannot be caught (no "before"). **Limitation:** on animals of
identical size the size windows are noise, and the cosine of noise-only means
dips just past -1 now and then (2 of 24 contacts in the synthetic fixture of
`tests/e2e/id-switch-image-check.mjs`). Analysis after embedding takes seconds
per session. (Those sessions also carry `*.predictions.static.externals.slp`
per camera, which a harness must leave out of the folder pick.)

**Coat brightness keeps the classifier path (single camera).**
`checkBrightnessSwitches` runs the scoring the image check had before #309
(`checkClassifierSwitches`: a per-view classifier fitted to the tracker's labels,
`trackletEncounters`, `testMoments`) on a 4-number vector per animal and view —
the 10th / 50th / 90th percentile and mean grey level at its body keypoints,
log-scaled (`ui/brightness-sampler.js`). Per pair, a run of flagged encounters
gives one change point — an onset when it reaches the session end, an 'end' when
it starts it, both for a middle run — naming the other edge of its swapped
stretch (`switchBackAt` / `switchedAt`); other members are repeats
(`continues`), and a change point within `followSeconds` after another sharing
an identity is its follow-on (`followOf`). On one camera
(`singleCameraCheckOptions`): `skipEmpty` (an encounter scoring exactly 0 had no
samples, so it neither ends nor continues a run) and `clearestEndLeads` (among
nearby 'end' rows sharing an animal, the clearest leads).
- **Candidate moments** (`opts.moments`, from `candidateMoments`: tracker
  near-ties and input-tracklet changes) are tested on BOTH sides (`testMoments`,
  15 s each, -200; `momentChangePoints`). Reading only after a moment caught 1
  more switch: the classifier learns whichever labelling covers more of the
  video. A moment's row is never a follow-on, other pairs' rows up to 3 s before
  it follow it, and it merges with the flagged run it starts or ends — one
  swapped stretch, one row, one Fix. An 'end' with no run before it reads back
  through the pair's encounters still scoring below `continueBelow` (the run
  hysteresis, going backwards) to the last that reads right, and a later 'end' of
  the same pair with nothing reading right in between adds no row (same stretch);
  two moments of the same pair and side within 3 s (a near-tie and a return at one
  contact) are one row with both cues and the clearer score.
  Measured 2026-10-27 by accepting each row's Fix as the dialog proposes it, alone,
  on Track All's labels (with returns): SLAP six 34 rows, Fixes +45.0 / -8.5 points
  summed -> 22 rows, +54.2 / -11.3; SLAP held-out 63 -> 58 rows, +488 / -4.6 ->
  +542 / -9.8; sham_gdx 13 -> 10 rows, +42 / -19.8 -> +61 / -21.1.
- **Calibrated on the 35 proofread single-camera SLAP videos (59 switches):**
  moment rows caught 14 with no false rows (the image check's moment rows under
  its old scoring: 13, 12 shared); encounter rows were noise at every threshold,
  hence `threshold` -800. Not calibrated on multi-camera data.

**On one camera (2026-10-12), images**, at -0.8, top and mid views of all 22
proofread SLAP sessions (87 views) and topC: original six 16 rows (14 real, 2
naming the wrong pair), 14 of 44 swaps found; held-out 75 rows (59 real, 14
wrong pair, 2 false), 68 of 232; topC both swaps, 3 false rows; no row at all on
the 39 views Track All gets >= 99.9% right. 113 of the 276 swaps are one track
changing animal alone, which no contact check can see. Harness:
`tests/e2e/_real-single-camera.mjs` (`IMAGE=1`), scored in a session scratchpad.

**Imports from project modules.** `pose/pose-data.js` (`readPoint3d`).

**Imported by.** `ui/id-switch-modal.js`, `ui/image-embedder.js` (`planKeyframeSamples`, `KEYFRAME_GAP_TOLERANCE`),
`ui/brightness-sampler.js` (`planKeyframeSamples`).

**Coverage.** `tests/test-id-switch-check.mjs` (synthetic sessions: clean -> no
flags; a switch late or early (the swapped stretch the MAJORITY); 25-120 fps
invariance; images catching a swap between animals of IDENTICAL size that size
misses; a pair swapping three times from its first encounter; huddle and
three-animal scenarios; the identity metric on look-alikes; `symmetricEigen`;
`markContactChanges`; cancellation; PCA; failure reasons; progress; crop
geometry of `ui/image-embedder.js`; `planKeyframeSamples`; the sample plan: every sample
the scoring reads is planned and scores match embedding all, a dropped window sample
fails it (negative control), one round with `croppable` and the same result after a
second without, keyframe placement, the round cap; sample weights in the metric),
`tests/e2e/image-keyframe-sampling.mjs`, `tests/e2e/size-switch-check.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/track-auto-size-switch-check.mjs`, `tests/e2e/id-switch-image-check.mjs`.

### pose/embedding-cache.js

**Purpose.** The store behind the image ID-switch check's repeat runs
(`ui/image-embedder.js`): crop embeddings kept between runs, so a re-run after
Track All decodes, cuts and embeds only the crops whose pixels can have changed.
Pure — no DOM, no app state.

**Key exports.** `cropKeyHash(g, others)` -> 16 hex characters: a 64-bit hash
(two murmur3-style 32-bit mixes) of the EXACT IEEE-754 bits of everything
`cutCrop` reads besides the image — the crop geometry's `cx`, `cy`, `angle`,
`scale`, `L` (the mask's dilation) and `hull`, and every masked hull in order,
with lengths, so hull boundaries count. No rounding: two crops that rounded to one
key could differ by a pixel, and a hit must be the vector the model would give.
`g.pts` (the dialog's overlay keypoints) is ignored, as `cutCrop` ignores it.
`hullsReachingCrop(g, others, side)`: the masked hulls that can change the crop's
pixels — 3+ corners (what `cutCrop` draws) and a bounding box that, mapped into
the crop with `cutCrop`'s transform, comes within `MASK_REACH_MARGIN` (2 px, for
anti-aliasing) of the crop square; order kept, a NaN corner counts as reaching.
Only these go into the key, so a neighbour moving far away keeps the crop's
entry (on the 5-mouse recording 15% of a re-run's crops missed only for that);
`cutCrop` itself still gets every hull. Lossless pixel for pixel
(`tests/e2e/image-check-cache.mjs` §5).
`EmbeddingCache({maxEntries, chunk})`: a bounded LRU of fixed-length Float32
vectors by string key — `get(key)` (a COPY, now most recently used, or null),
`put(key, vec)` (stores a copy; evicts the least recently used when full; a
vector of another length clears it), `has`, `deleteWhere(pred)`, `clear()`,
`size`, `bytes`, `dim`. Vectors live in pooled `Float32Array` chunks of
`EMBED_CACHE_CHUNK` (4096) slots, grown on demand and freed when emptied — never
one array per vector (the renderer's V8 heap cap). `EMBED_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES`
(65,536 = 96 MB at 384 floats; a 15-min, 5-animal, 3-view run is ~25k).

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/image-embedder.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-embedding-cache.mjs` (one bit of any input changes the
key, overlay points don't, no collisions over 20k near-identical geometries;
which hulls reach the crop at different rotations; LRU
order, slot recycling, copies, pooling, freeing); end to end
`tests/e2e/image-check-cache.mjs`.

---

### pose/view-align.js

**Purpose.** The math behind View ▸ **Align Views to References…**:
given two or more reference views the user has rotated the way they like,
compute the in-plane display rotation (`view.rotation` degrees) for every other
calibrated camera so the scene looks the same way round in all of them. Pure,
import-free (takes `Camera`-shaped objects), so it runs as-is in Node.

**Key exports.** `alignViewRotations(cameras, rotations, referenceNames, {center?})`
-> `{ok, center, rotations, skipped:[{name, reason}], disagreementDeg}` or
`{ok:false, error}`; `estimateSceneCenter(cameras)` (least-squares point closest
to every optical axis); geometry helpers `cameraCenterWorld`, `opticalAxisWorld`,
`projectToImage` (pinhole + `distortPoint`, `null` behind the camera),
`backProjectToWorldDir`, `screenUpImageDir`, `rotationForImageDir`, `wrapDeg`;
constants `UP_PULL`, `MAX_LINE_OF_SIGHT_ALIGNMENT`, `UPSIDE_DOWN_DEG`.

**The model** is documented in the file header: each reference contributes its
screen-up PLANE and its 3D up vector; per camera, `U` is the unit vector best
fitting the planes with a proximity-weighted pull towards the references' ups,
and the view is rotated so `U` projects screen-up at the scene centre. Distinct
planes -> their intersection (a ring of side cameras gets gravity up exactly);
coincident planes (a central top + central front pair) -> the weighted SUM of
the references' ups.

**Notes / caveats.**
- **Two earlier models failed on real rigs** and are documented in the header
  so they are not reintroduced: a single global plane intersection (refuses the
  natural top + front pair, whose planes coincide); carrying the reference's
  orientation along the shortest orbit (twisted ring cameras up to upside down).
- **The tie-break must be the SUM of up vectors, not squared alignments** — a
  top view's up is horizontal and a front view's vertical; only their sum
  projects up in both.
- **Planes are not distance-weighted** (only the tie-break is): down-weighting a
  far reference's plane let the tie-break drag a clean intersection ~4° off.
- **At least two references.** With one, `U` is that reference's own up, which
  leaves ring cameras 10–20° off when it looks slightly down.
- **The scene centre is calibration-only** (optical axes), so it behaves the
  same on a lazily-loaded project whose frames are not resident; it refuses rigs
  whose axes are all within ~2° of parallel.
- A camera looking within 5° of `U` is skipped (its rotation would be noise), as
  is one with the scene centre behind it.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/view-align-modal.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-view-align.mjs` (synthetic rigs, every aligned view
checked by projecting through the real `Camera` and rotating the way CSS does)
and `tests/e2e/align-views-to-references.mjs` (the dialog in the real app).
Real-data check: `tests/e2e/_diag-real-align-views.mjs`.

---

## ui/

### ui/app-state.js

**Purpose.** Central application state and controller-singleton registry.
Exports `state` (mutable shared bag) plus five live-binding controllers
(`videoController`, `interactionManager`, `viewport3d`, `timeline`,
`paneManager`) updated through setter functions. Exposes
`window.__lucid` for DevTools inspection.

**One skeleton per project.** The pose skeleton is project-level: every
`state.sessions[*].skeleton` points at ONE shared object, so it can't diverge
across sessions and the exported `.slp` always carries a single skeleton (no
duplicates for sleap-io/sleap-nn). `setProjectSkeleton(sk)` points all sessions
at `sk` and stores it as the default new sessions inherit; `getProjectSkeleton()`
returns it; `buildRememberedSkeleton()` returns that SHARED reference (not an
independent clone). The editor mutates the shared object in place so edits
propagate for free; node add/remove additionally fan out via
`propagateNode{Added,Removed}` across each session's instances (see
`ui/info-panel.js` → `applyProjectSkeleton` / warn-on-overwrite modal).
Calibration and `envSkeleton` remain per-session.

**Key exports.**
- `state` — mutable application state (current frame, sessions, dirty
  flag, view list, color mode, etc.).
  `state.trailLength` (node-trail frames) is an **accessor**, not a field: it
  reads `trailFrames(state.trailSeconds, state.fps)` (`ui/trail-presets.js`), so
  it follows the frame rate with no caller recomputing it. Setting it (tests and
  benches pin a frame count) stores `frames / fps` in `trailSeconds`. The
  pickers set `trailSeconds`.
  `state.trailShade` — the node-trail shade, a `TRAIL_SHADES` key
  (`ui/overlays.js`), starting at `DEFAULT_TRAIL_SHADE` ('dark'); `ui/ui-wiring.js`
  replaces it at startup with the one remembered in `localStorage`.
  `state.nativeResolution` is View ▸ Native Resolution (Shift+0): true exactly
  when every shown view is at one video pixel per device pixel. Written only by
  `VideoController._syncNativeMode` (derived from the views, never latched).
  Read by `VideoController.initZoom` (a view's first zoom state starts at 1:1
  while it is on) and by `refreshPaneInteractions` (a view coming on screen
  while it is on joins it at 1:1 — see `ui/sessions-panes.js`).
- `videoController`, `interactionManager`, `viewport3d`, `timeline`,
  `paneManager` — live `let` bindings.
- `setVideoController`, `setInteractionManager`, `setViewport3D`,
  `setTimeline`, `setPaneManager`.
- `hasRealVideo()` — true only when a view actually has a decoder. A non-null
  `videoController` is NOT sufficient: `setupEmptyVideoController()` installs one
  at app init, and a skeleton + imported-3D-points project keeps that empty
  controller. Frame navigation / playback branch on this, not on the
  controller's existence (used by `navigateToFrame`, the transport buttons, and
  the keyboard handler so play/pause + stepping work without video).
- `isViewDocked(view)` — true when the view has a pane in the dock
  (`paneManager.dockedViews`; a pane in an inactive tab counts), or when there
  is no dock yet. Fed to `VideoController` as `callbacks.isViewShown` and read by
  `drawAllOverlays`, so stepping, playback and overlay drawing skip the views
  nobody can see — one camera solo'd, or panes closed (a solo'd camera on a
  17-camera project steps ~9x faster). Safe because docking a view again builds
  a NEW canvas, whose renderer re-seeks the current frame
  (`refreshPaneInteractions`), so a skipped view never shows a stale picture.
  Covered by `tests/e2e/solo-view-decodes-shown-only.mjs`.
- `VIEW_NAMES` — `['back', 'mid', 'side', 'top']`.
- `getActiveSession()`, `setActiveSession(session)`.
- `getProjectSkeleton()` / `setProjectSkeleton(skeleton)` — see above.
- `rememberSkeleton(skeleton)` / `buildRememberedSkeleton()` — the project
  skeleton newly loaded videos/sessions inherit instead of starting blank.
  `rememberSkeleton` stores the REFERENCE (not a clone) and ignores empty
  skeletons; `buildRememberedSkeleton` returns that shared reference (or null).
  Module-level state: carries across video loads within one app session, resets
  on a full page reload (no persistence).
- `setInstanceClipboard(data)` / `getInstanceClipboard()` — in-memory clipboard
  for the instance copy/paste feature (Cmd/Ctrl+C / Cmd/Ctrl+V). Holds a copied
  UserInstance as `{ compatKey, pointsByName: { name -> {point, occluded} },
  sourceView, sourceFrame }`, so paste can remap points by node name onto a
  matching skeleton. Same lifetime model as the remembered skeleton (app session
  only). Filled/read by `copySelectedInstance`/`pasteInstance` in `ui-wiring.js`.

**Imports from project modules.** `./trail-presets.js` (`trailFrames`,
`trailRate`) and `./overlays.js` (`DEFAULT_TRAIL_SHADE`), both import-free.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `pose/triangulation.js`,
`pose/tracker.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `import-export/plane-metadata.js`,
`loading/session-loader.js`, and most of `ui/` (info-panel, rendering,
identity-assignment, export-modals, sessions-panes, layout-controls, ui-wiring,
view-align-modal, the plane panels, origin-definition / origin-rebase,
id-switch-modal / id-switch-highlight, image-embedder, identity-gallery,
timeline-controller, track-range-modal, settings-modal, skeleton-edit-warning,
animal-count-field, frame-readout, view-legend, overlay-export-modal, …).

**User-facing features.** Backs literally everything — session switching,
playback state, dirty tracking, multi-session UI.

**Key exports.** `setColorByIdentity(state, on)` -> `true` if it changed
anything (the handler runs only on a change); `onColorByChange(fn)` —
registers the single change handler (ui-wiring: button highlight +
`drawAllOverlays` + `update3DViewport` + the timeline's track rows and the
Visibility tab's track swatches). With no handler (Node tests) it only
sets the state.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (registers the handler; the Tracks / Identity
buttons route through `setColorByIdentity`), `pose/tracker.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-tracker-gui.mjs` (Track All flips it once, a second

### ui/version-label.js

**Purpose.** Which build this is, at the right end of the menu bar beside
Settings (`#menuBarVersion`): `v1.0.5` on the live page. It knows no version
itself — `scripts/stamp-version.mjs` fills the `lucid-release` and
`lucid-commit` `<meta>` tags in the DEPLOYED copy of `index.html`, with the label
the deploy workflow chose: the release tag when the deployed ref is one, else
`dev` or `PR #<n>`. A tag is shown alone; any other label gets the 8-character
commit beside it (`dev · 67978a8b`), since that is what tells two `/dev/`
deploys — or a stale Cloudflare cache — apart. Unstamped (a working tree, both
tags empty) the element stays `hidden`. The label is selectable
(`user-select: text`, unlike the rest of the menu bar) because it is what a bug
report quotes.

**Key exports.** `formatVersionLabel(release, commit)` -> `{text, title}` or
`null` (DOM-free); `showVersionLabel(doc = document)` — fills, titles and
shows/hides `#menuBarVersion` from the `<meta>` tags; a page without the
element is left alone.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`setupMenus`, beside the Docs / Settings
buttons).

**Coverage.** `tests/test-version-label.mjs` (every channel's label and the
hidden case), `tests/test-stamp-version.mjs` (`stampBuildInfo`, the label
validation, and a stamp failing when the tags are gone) and
`tests/e2e/stamped-build.mjs` (a stamped copy shows the label right of Settings,
selectable).

### ui/browser-hints.js

**Purpose.** Browser-specific advice for features a browser has turned off.
Brave ships the File System Access API (`showSaveFilePicker`,
`showDirectoryPicker`, `showOpenFilePicker`) DISABLED by default while
identifying as Chrome, so in Brave saves fall back to an in-memory download
(risky for a large project, never saved in place), large exports must be
buffered in memory, and multi-session loading refuses. It is enabled at
`brave://flags/#file-system-access-api`. Safari and Firefox can't enable the
API, so they get no hint (existing messages stand).

**Brave's fingerprinting protection ("farbling").** With Shields at their
default, Brave reports a RANDOM core count to each site
(`navigator.hardwareConcurrency`, between 2 and the true count, fixed per site
for a session) and adds noise to canvas pixel reads; a per-site exception
(Shields > Advanced controls > Allow fingerprinting) stops both. It matters
because the worker pools are sized from the core count (Triangulate All ~5x
slower at Brave's floor of 2), the CPU line divides by it, and the image
ID-switch check reads its crops from a canvas. The app cannot learn the true
count, so it tells the user how to give it: a note under the CPU line of the
progress popups. **Detection is the canvas read, not the count**: a draw can
land on the true count and the noise is still there.

**Key exports.**
- `isBrave()` — `navigator.brave.isBrave` present.
- `hasFileSystemAccess()` — save + directory pickers available.
- `fileSystemAccessHint()` — how to enable the API (paste
  `brave://flags/#file-system-access-api`, set Enabled, relaunch — or use
  Chrome/Edge); `''` unless Brave with the API off, so callers can append it.
- `canvasReadsPerturbed()` — a known 64 x 64 pattern written with
  `putImageData` and read straight back comes back changed. Checked once per
  page and cached: it cannot change without a reload, which is what changing
  Shields does. `__setCanvasProbe(fn)` replaces it for tests.
- `braveFingerprintingActive()` — Brave AND canvas reads perturbed.
- `braveFingerprintingHint(cores)` -> `{lead, why, steps, after}` | null —
  "Brave reports a random number of CPU cores to this site (this session: N)"
  ("random", not "wrong": the draw can be the true count), what that does here,
  three steps (save first, since changing Shields reloads the page; the
  Shields icon; Advanced controls ▸ Allow fingerprinting) and an after-note.
  The labels are Brave 1.97's own. null unless `braveFingerprintingActive()`.
- `renderBraveFingerprintingHint(el, cores)` — fills `el` (a `.progress-hint`)
  with the bold lead, a numbered list and the after-note, built from text
  nodes, and shows it; hides and empties it when there is no hint.

**Imports from project modules.** None. No DOM at load (the canvas probe runs
on first use, on the main thread), so it is safe to import anywhere.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js` (multi-session load message),
`import-export/save-load.js` (`saveProjectSlp` download fallback status),
`ui/export-modals.js` (3D video + JSON export in-memory confirms),
`ui/overlay-export-modal.js` (overlay video in-memory confirm),
`ui/loading-overlay.js` (`setLoadingCpu`: the note under the loading card's CPU
line, `#loadingBraveHint`), `ui/id-switch-modal.js` (the image check's dialog,
under its CPU and GPU lines).

**Tests.** `tests/test-browser-hints.js`; `tests/e2e/brave-fingerprinting-hint.mjs`
drives REAL Brave (skips when Brave is not installed, `BRAVE=` overrides the
path): the note with default Shields, none with the per-site exception, none
in Chromium.

### ui/folder-confirm-tip.js

**Purpose.** On a Mac, say how to confirm the browser's folder prompt from the
keyboard, just before a folder picker opens. After a folder is picked,
Chromium-based browsers ask again — "Upload N files to this site?" for
`<input webkitdirectory>`, "Let site view files?" for `showDirectoryPicker` —
with Cancel as the focused AND default button plus input protection, so Return
cancels the load and no page can change that. On macOS, Tab reaches the other
button only with System Settings ▸ Keyboard ▸ Keyboard navigation on (off by
default); then Space presses it. **No web API exposes that setting**, so the
tip goes to every Mac user of a Chromium browser before each folder pick until
they tick "Don't show this again" (`localStorage` `folderConfirmTipDismissed` —
browser-local display preference, never project state). Chromium is recognised
by `navigator.userAgentData` (Safari and Firefox never see it). An automated
browser (`navigator.webdriver`) never sees it either: the e2e suites run on
Macs and drive these loaders.

The tip GATES the picker (Cancel / Continue) rather than sitting beside it:
the browser's prompt is tab-modal, so nothing on the page is clickable while it
is up. Continue — or Return — is fresh user activation for the picker, which
matters once the menu click's own (5 s in Chromium) has expired while the user
reads. Esc cancels. Return anywhere but a focused button means Continue, so a
pointer click on the checkbox (which releases focus to the page) does not
strand it.

**Key exports.**
- `confirmFolderPick()` -> `Promise<boolean>` — what callers await before a
  folder picker: true at once where the tip does not apply, otherwise the
  tip's answer (false = cancelled).
- `shouldShowFolderConfirmTip(nav?, storage?)` — Chromium on macOS, not
  automated, not dismissed; storage that throws counts as not dismissed.
- `isChromiumOnMac(nav?)` — `navigator.userAgentData.platform === 'macOS'`.
- `showFolderConfirmTip()` -> `Promise<boolean>` — the modal itself
  (`#folderTipOverlay`, `.plane-confirm-modal.folder-tip-modal`, checkbox
  `#folderTipDontShow`, buttons `#btnFolderTipCancel` / `#btnFolderTipContinue`).
- `FOLDER_TIP_STORAGE_KEY`.

**Imports from project modules.** None. No DOM at load, so Node can import it.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-folder-confirm-tip.mjs` (who sees it) and
`tests/e2e/folder-confirm-tip.mjs` (the real menu items, incl. user activation
still present when the picker is called 5.5 s after the menu click, and
Windows / automated macOS never seeing it).

### ui/custom-delete-ops.js

**Purpose.** Pure, DOM-free logic behind "Custom Instance Delete…" — LUCID's
equivalent of SLEAP's `sleap/gui/dialogs/delete.py` `DeleteDialog`
(Labels ▸ Custom Instance Delete…), adapted to the multi-view model. Imports **no
project modules** (only calls methods on the `Session` it is handed), so it bridges
into `tests/test-runner.html` for isolated unit testing — same contract as
`ui/track-identity-ops.js`. The DOM modal lives in `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**Exports.** `collectDeletionTargets(session, filters, ctx)` (pure — returns
`{targets, count, byCamera, groupsDissolved, groupsUngrouped, instancesPromoted,
groupsLosing3d}`), `previewCascade(targets)`, `executeDeletion(session, targets)`,
`pruneOrphanIdentities(session, frameIndices)`, and the durable half shared with
the interactive deletes: `deleteTargetsFromStore(session, targets)` (→ `{durable,
errorRows, firstError, touchedFrames}`) plus the two target builders it is fed by
`ui/interaction.js` and `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `unlinkedTarget(frameIdx, ul)` and
`groupMemberTargets(frameIdx, group, camNames?)`.

**Every delete of a store-backed instance goes through `deleteTargetsFromStore`,
not only this dialog** — also the Delete key / Edit ▸ Delete Instance / the
toolbar's "- Instance" (`InteractionManager._deleteSelected`) and the group
context menu's "Delete group". Editing the RESIDENT frame alone is undone on a
lazy project twice over: a windowed sweep or playback eviction re-hydrates the
row, and the streaming save writes the store rows of any camera-frame with no
user instance. The helper must run BEFORE the caller's memory edit: it
recognises the victims by their `_rawInstIndex` while they are still in the
group/frame, and renumbers the survivors around them. A target with no `rawIdx`
(an instance added this session) has no row and never reaches the store; an
eager session has no store and the call is a no-op apart from that renumbering.
It passes `deleteInstanceRows` an `only` map, so one keypress walks one
camera-frame instead of every row of the project.

**Vocabulary.** SLEAP's *video* scope is LUCID's **session** — every camera in a
session shares one frame index space, so a camera is a VIEW FILTER, not a frame
domain. The UI says **Grouped / Ungrouped** (the panel headers) and must never say
"Unlinked": in SLEAP an *unlinked prediction* is one with no `from_predicted`
back-link to a user instance, an unrelated concept that shares the word.

**Identity is resolved PER FRAME** via `session.getIdentityIdForTrack(cam,
trackIdx, frameIdx)`, never `group.identityId` (only refreshed on the frame an
identity was assigned). This is the same call `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll`
buckets by, so the dialog and the grouping cannot disagree. A TRACKLESS
instance has no key in that map, so `_identityMatches` falls back to its
instance-level `inst.identityId` (`>= 0`, stamped by `unlinkGroup`), mirroring
`Session.getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance` — otherwise `identityMode:'none'`
(the "delete instances with no ID" sweep) would delete an animal the user had
just labeled.

**Type and grouping are ORTHOGONAL axes**, not siblings — a grouped instance is
still user or predicted. "Delete grouped instances" is `type:'all'` +
`grouping:'grouped'`. Conflating them is the main way this dialog could become
incoherent. `type:'all'` = user + predicted (matching SLEAP's "all instances"),
never reprojections — those are derived and live in `reprojectedInstances`.

**Lazy / not-yet-hydrated frames.** Scope enumeration must never loop
`session.frameGroups` — on a lazy project that is the small resident window, so
a bulk delete driven from it would silently delete almost nothing while
reporting success. So:
- `frameScope:'currentSession'` is **store-driven** via
  `lazyLoader.forEachInstanceRow`, with no hydration and no `async`: **type/track**
  from the store's own columns (`forEachInstanceRow`'s 4th `info` argument),
  **identity** from `frameIdentityMap`, and **grouping** from
  `session.instanceGroups` (project-wide; members carry `_rawInstIndex`).
- `frameScope:'currentFrame'` normally reads the resident model, but falls
  back to the same store-driven collector restricted to that one frame when the
  frame has **no `FrameGroup`** (never hydrated) — otherwise its ungrouped rows,
  which exist only in the store, would be missed while grouped members were
  still found.
- A non-resident row has no `UnlinkedInstance` wrapper; `executeDeletion` counts it
  and skips the pool update (the frame hydrates from the compacted store).
- With no `lazyLoader` at all, session scope walks `frameGroups` ∪ `instanceGroups`;
  an eager project is fully resident, so that enumeration is complete by definition.

**Cascade, and why the dialog reports it.** A group must have ≥2 members, so
removing members can dissolve a group (`removeInstanceGroup`), auto-ungroup it
(`unlinkGroup`, returning the lone survivor to the ungrouped pool and **promoting a
predicted survivor of a formerly-mixed group to `type:'user', modified:true`**), or
leave it intact with stale 3D. `previewCascade` predicts all of this before any
mutation so the modal can warn — reporting only "N instances" hides two
irreversible side effects.

**`executeDeletion` order is load-bearing**: (1) `lazyLoader.deleteInstanceRows` — the persistence, and it must run BEFORE
`_rawInstIndex` is touched since the row identity *is* `_rawInstIndex`;
(2) renumber `_rawInstIndex` on survivors, else `refFor` writes grouping refs at the
wrong instances and `finalizeLazyFrameGroup` hydrates the wrong 2D — (1) and (2)
are `deleteTargetsFromStore`;
(3) `instanceGroups` cascade; (4) `frameGroups` cascade under the same `seen` Set
(hydrated frames share instance objects between the maps);
(5) prune `frameIdentityMap`. Never assigns `group.observedPoints` (a read-only
getter — assigning throws in every ES module). App-level triangulation caches
are the caller's job: run `purgeTriangulationDataForGroup` over the returned
`purgedGroups`.

**`pruneOrphanIdentities` is not cosmetic.** `frameIdentityMap` is serialized into
the `.slp`, so residue can re-attach a ghost identity to a later instance reusing
the same `(frame, camera, track)`; and `ensureGroupsFromIdentities` RECREATES groups
from this map for any frame with no `instanceGroups` entry, so an unpruned entry
brings a deleted group back on the next Triangulate All. Goes through
`session.deleteFrameIdentity` because the keys are **packed Numbers** — a raw
`frameIdx + ':' + cam + ':' + track` string comparison silently matches
nothing.

**Imports from project modules.** None (deliberately).

**Tests.** `tests/test-custom-delete-ops.js` (incl. `InteractionManager._deleteSelected`
reaching a stub store), `tests/test-custom-delete-store.js` (the store
primitive), and `tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs` cycle 5c (the five
interactive delete shapes, through sweep + save + reopen).

### ui/frame-counters.js

**Purpose.** Pure, DOM-free logic behind the status bar's **Labeled Frames**,
**Instances** and **Triangulated** counters; `updateFrameCounters`
(`ui/rendering.js`) is the DOM/scheduling half. Imports **no project modules**
— it only reads the `Session` it is handed — so a Node test drives it with real
`pose/pose-data.js` objects and a real `SioLazyLoader` (same contract as
`ui/custom-delete-ops.js`), and it bridges into both browser-suite runners.

**The counting rules**: a frame is labeled in the active camera if it holds a
user instance, grouped or not, or a GROUPED prediction — an ungrouped
prediction is raw tracker output and does not count; **Instances** counts user
instances in that camera; **Triangulated** counts frames with at least one
`InstanceGroup` carrying `points3d`, in any camera (including a frame with 3D
but no 2D `FrameGroup`).

**Why it is not a walk of `session.frameGroups`.** On a lazy project that map is
the resident window, so a walk reports a plausible, tiny number. A count is
split in two:
- **Resident frames** are counted LIVE from their `FrameGroup`. They are what is
  on screen, they carry in-memory edits the store does not know about, and the
  current frame is one of them, so an edit shows up immediately.
- **Every other frame** comes from a per-frame **baseline** built without
  hydrating anything: Triangulated straight off `session.instanceGroups` (never
  windowed); Labeled / Instances from the columnar store via
  `lazyLoader.forEachInstanceRow({camera, start, end})` plus `instanceGroups`
  membership, **mirroring `finalizeLazyFrameGroup`** (`pose/triangulation.js`):
  a row whose in-frame offset is some member's `_rawInstIndex` is that member,
  with the MEMBER's type; a member with no `_rawInstIndex`, or one past the
  frame's last row, is dropped; every other row is ungrouped, with the store's
  type. So the baseline is "what the frame would count as if it were visited
  now", and `tests/test-frame-counters.mjs` checks it against the real
  hydration path.

The baseline is per-FRAME, not a total, so resident frames can be taken back
out of it: `total − Σbaseline(resident) + Σlive(resident)`. That is
O(resident) per call and exact whatever the resident set is — scrubbing,
eviction and sweeps' window release need no rebuild. Only a change to frames
that are NOT resident (a bulk operation) does.

**An EAGER project counts from a baseline too — and re-counts only the drawn
frame.** There every frame is resident, so "resident frames live" would walk
the WHOLE project on every frame step (the largest cost of a step on a big
eager project). The builder gives an eager project (no lazy loader) a camera
half built from the `FrameGroup`s with `residentFrameUserCount`, marked
`eager: true`; `countFrameCounters` then returns the baseline's totals with no
walk, and `refreshCountedFrame(session, baseline, f)` — which
`updateFrameCounters` calls for the drawn frame on every update — re-counts
frame `f` into EVERY camera half held and into the 3D half: O(cameras). That
keeps it exact through edits, which are always to the frame on screen and
always followed by a redraw of it. A bulk operation ends with a same-frame
redraw, after which `updateFrameCounters` rebuilds the baseline, as on a lazy
project; on an eager project that rebuild DROPS the other views' halves (every
frame being resident, `nonResidentCameraCounts` cannot tell an edit from a bulk
change), and each is rebuilt when its view is next active.

**Exports.**
- `residentFrameUserCount(fg, cam)` -> `-1` if the frame is not labeled in
  `cam`, else its user-instance count (one number, so the per-frame loop
  allocates nothing).
- `groupsHaveTriangulation(groups)` -> boolean.
- `createFrameCounterBaselineBuilder(session, cam, {tri}?)` -> `{step(budget),
  result}` — the baseline as a RESUMABLE build (~100 ms at 108,000 frames x 8
  cameras, so the caller spreads it over short tasks). `step` does about
  `budget` frames / `instanceGroups` entries and returns true when done.
  `result` = `{tri: {byFrame: Uint8Array, total}, cams: Map<cam, {byFrame:
  Int32Array, labeledTotal, usersTotal}>, eager}`; on an eager project the
  camera half comes from the `FrameGroup`s and `eager` is true; the camera half
  is absent when the project is lazy but its loader cannot enumerate rows (the
  worker-backed analysis-`.h5` loader — every frame with data is then
  resident). `tri: false`
  skips the 3D half. The session is read live between steps; a change mid-build
  can leave the result stale, and the caller rebuilds after changes anyway.
- `computeFrameCounterBaseline(session, cam)` / `computeLazyCameraBaseline(session, cam)`
  — the same, run to completion (whole baseline / camera half only).
- `countFrameCounters(session, cam, baseline)` -> `{labeled, instances,
  triangulated}`. With an eager baseline holding `cam`'s half (or no `cam`):
  the baseline's totals, no walk. With no baseline (or none for `cam`),
  Labeled / Instances are the resident-only count.
- `refreshCountedFrame(session, baseline, f)` — re-count frame `f` into an
  EAGER baseline in place (every camera half + the 3D half, totals adjusted;
  arrays grow for a frame past their end). A no-op on a lazy baseline.
- `nonResidentCameraCounts(session, half)` -> `{labeled, instances}` — the part
  of a camera half that `countFrameCounters` actually uses. Two builds that
  differ here saw a change to non-resident frames (a bulk operation), which
  `updateFrameCounters` takes as its cue to drop the other views' halves.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/rendering.js`; bridged as `window.__FrameCounters` by
`tests/test-runner.html`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-frame-counters.mjs` (lazy counts equal a fully-hydrated
count through the real `batchLoadLazyFrames`; eager counting iterates no
frames), `tests/e2e/status-counters-eager.mjs` (the real status bar on an
eager project), `tests/test-bottom-bar.js` (the rules, against this module),
and `tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs`'s `checkCounters` (a lazily
reopened project).

### ui/frame-readout.js

**Purpose.** The controls bar's frame readout, left of the transport buttons:
the current time / duration on top, the current frame / total frames below,
every count with thousands separators.

    00:41.100 / 20:00.000
        1,234 / 36,000

**Key exports.** `formatFrameNumber(n)` (`36000` -> `"36,000"`, one cached
`Intl.NumberFormat('en-US')`, since it runs on every frame of playback),
`frameReadoutText(frameIdx, totalFrames, fps)` -> `{frame, total, time,
duration}` (pure; `frameIdx` null = the empty "0 / 0" state), `NO_TIME`
(`--:--.---`), and the DOM half: `showReadoutFrame(frameIdx)` (the per-frame
path — writes `#currentFrame` / `#currentTime`), `refreshReadoutTotals()`
(re-reads `state.totalFrames` / `state.fps`, writes `#totalFrames` /
`#totalTime`, re-times the frame on screen and refreshes the seekbar's time
ruler) and `clearReadout()` (which empties the ruler too).

**Behaviour.**
- **It speaks the seekbar tooltip's language**, on purpose: the same separator,
  the same `formatTimestamp`, and the same time — a frame's START time,
  `frameIdx / state.fps`, at the playback FPS the FPS pill shows. So a click on
  the seekbar puts the tooltip's exact text into this readout. The duration is
  `totalFrames / fps`, so the last frame reads one frame short of it. An
  unknown frame rate (`state.fps` is 0 while a multi-session load has no video
  yet) shows `NO_TIME` rather than a made-up clock.
- **Every writer of the readout goes through here** — `updateSeekbarVisual`,
  the inline frame editor and FPS pill (`ui/ui-wiring.js`), `updateFpsDisplay`
  (`pose/initialization.js`), the loaders (`loading/session-loader.js`,
  `import-export/slp-import.js`), session switch / removal
  (`ui/sessions-panes.js`) and New Project (`import-export/save-load.js`) — so
  the time row cannot fall out of step with the frame row. A new path that
  changes `state.totalFrames` or `state.fps` must call `refreshReadoutTotals`.
- The inline frame editor accepts the number the way the readout shows it:
  `"12,345"` seeks to frame 12,345 (commas and spaces are stripped).
- Layout (`.frame-display` in `styles.css`) is a three-column grid — current
  values right-aligned, totals left-aligned — so the two slashes stay in one
  vertical line whatever the digit counts; both rows fit the 56 px controls bar.

**Imports from project modules.** `./app-state.js` (`state`),
`./seekbar-tooltip.js` (`formatTimestamp`), `./seekbar-ticks.js`
(`refreshSeekbarTicks` — the ruler marks the same times, so it follows every
totals / FPS change, including the FPS pill's, which moves no frame) — all
import-free, so the text half runs in Node.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/sessions-panes.js`,
`pose/initialization.js`, `loading/session-loader.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-frame-readout.mjs` (the text, incl. agreement with
`seekbarTooltipText`) and `tests/e2e/frame-readout.mjs` (real app: seekbar
clicks, alignment and fit at 1400 and 640 px, the frame editor, an FPS edit).

### ui/export-modals.js

**Purpose.** Modal dialogs for bulk-triangulation and export (Group-by-Track,
Group-by-Identity, multi-frame triangulation, SLP per-session, SLP by-camera,
SLP all-sessions, JSON labels, points3d H5, reproj H5).

**Key exports.**
- `showGroupByTrackModal()` — modal that bulk-groups by trackIdx, then
  triangulates (`groupByTrackAndTriangulateAll`). Keeps only the 3D: it drops
  every frame's derived state before regrouping
  (`dropAllDerivedTriangulationState`) and stores no reprojections or
  `state.triangulationResults`; its timeline pass walks the frames it
  regrouped (see "Nor does any other bulk path" under
  `pose/triangulation.js`). Solves with Settings ▸ Default Triangulation
  (`resolveTriangulationMethod(null)`). A frame the sweep visits with no
  FrameGroup (3D grouping, no 2D — `sweepLazyFrameWindows`' contract) is
  skipped and its groups left as they are, as in Group by Identity; without
  that guard it threw part-way through, leaving the project half regrouped.
- `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll(explicitMethod)` — bulk-group then
  triangulate. `explicitMethod` (`'ba'|'dlt'`) is the user's explicit pick when
  there was one — "Triangulate All ▸ DLT" routes here and passes `'dlt'`; omitting
  it (Tracks ▸ Group by Identity) means "use Settings ▸ Default Triangulation".
  Either way the resolved method **governs the whole sweep**. Ends by
  calling `update3DViewport(state.currentFrame)` so the 3D viewer populates for
  the current frame (this is the path "Triangulate All" takes when identities
  exist). Its **unlinked** rows bucket via
  `session.getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance` rather than the track-keyed
  resolver — a trackless ungrouped instance keeps its identity on the instance,
  so asking by track would drop exactly the instances an ungroup produced.
  Mirrors the identical rule in `ensureGroupsFromIdentities`
  (`pose/triangulation.js`), whose bucketing this duplicates.

  **Both grouping sweeps ADOPT existing 3D rather than re-solving it.** Each
  rebuilds a frame's `InstanceGroup`s around the SAME `Instance` objects; the
  fresh objects carry no `points3d` / `triangulationMethod`, so re-solving with
  `triangulateAndReproject`'s DEFAULT method would silently downgrade a **BA**
  project's 3D to DLT — and save/export read `group.points3d`.

  Each sweep resolves ONE governing method up front (the explicit pick, else
  Settings ▸ Default Triangulation) and every group ends up with 3D from that
  method. A group is ADOPTED — `findEquivalentPriorGroup` + `adoptPrior3d`, no
  solve — only when its membership is unchanged AND its existing 3D already
  came from that same method, i.e. only when re-solving is a provable no-op.
  Switching the default to BA re-solves the project (~3-6x the DLT cost); that
  is the user's explicit choice, so both sweeps name the method and report 3D
  provenance ("N kept existing 3D, N solved via Bundle Adjustment, N via DLT")
  in their progress text and status line.

  Guarded by `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-ba-export.mjs` (the exported 3D is
  bit-identical to an independent BA solve and NOT the DLT one; governance in
  both directions; an explicit `'dlt'` beats a BA default).

  `group.triangulationMethod` IS persisted (per-group
  `metadata.lucid.triangulationMethod`), so adoption works across a reopen too and
  a reopened BA project still displays BA's error — see `pose/pose-data.js`'s
  `InstanceGroup` entry.
- `sweepTriangulationFrames(session, onFrame, opts)` (module-private) — a thin
  wrapper over the shared `sweepLazyFrameWindows` (`pose/triangulation.js`),
  the memory-bounded driver both bulk-triangulate paths use: it visits every
  frame (not only resident ones), releasing each lazy window after use; 3D
  persists in `session.instanceGroups`. The JSON labels export (`exportLabels`)
  streams through the same sweep.
- `showExportResultPopup(message, ok)` (module-private) — small ✓/✗ card shown
  after a By-Cam / per-session SLP export (auto-dismisses; closes on
  click/Esc/Enter).
- `slpIncludeGroupHtml({predId, reprojId, reprojRowId, reprojToggleId})`
  (module-private) — markup for the **Include** group shared by the Per-Session
  and By-Cam SLP export modals: the app's standard `.toggle-switch`es, opened by
  a **note** (`.slp-inc-note`) reading "✓ UserLabels are automatically saved"
  above the two switches. Both modals always write user labels
  (`instanceFilter.user` is hardcoded `true`); without the note users read the
  silence as an exclusion. **A note, not a third always-on switch**: a control
  that cannot be operated misstates what the user can change. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/export-include-user-labels.mjs` (the note holds no control, exactly
  two option rows, each `.slider` really drives its checkbox — a
  `.toggle-switch` not wrapped in a `<label>` looks right and is dead — and a
  Per-Session export with both options OFF still writes every user instance).
- `showSlpExportModal()` — single-camera SLP export modal (pick one camera per
  session, export to one file). **Retained but not wired to the File menu**
  (replaced by `showSlpExportPerSessionModal`).
- `showSlpExportPerSessionModal()` — "Export SLEAP File Per Session": bulk export
  for the **open/active session only**. Lists every assigned-camera view in that
  session with a per-row **Download** checkbox (default ON; only checked rows are
  exported — an unchecked row gets `tr.slp-ps-off` and dims), camera, target
  directory, and versioned output filename
  `<stem>_vN.slp`, with the shared **Include** group (**Predicted Instances**
  default on; **Reprojections**, emitted as UserInstance/PredictedInstance via
  a sub-toggle that enables with it). On Export it **always prompts** for a
  folder (`window.showDirectoryPicker` — it does not silently reuse a cached
  `state.exportDirHandle`) and writes one 2D `.slp` per camera
  into that camera's associated subdirectory (`state.cameraDirMap[cam] || cam`),
  via `exportSlpClientSide(...)`. Versioned names mean source `.slp` files are
  never overwritten. Falls back to flat `downloadBlob` downloads when the File
  System Access API is unavailable. Esc closes the modal.
  The modal is `min(920px, 94vw)` (not a `min-width`, which beats `max-width`
  and pushes it off-screen on a narrow window); the filename field keeps the
  full name in `title` (the change handler re-syncs it). Row cells are
  **escaped** (`esc()`): rows are built by string concatenation from user-file
  names, and an unescaped `"` truncates the `value="…"` field.
- `showSlpExportByCamModal()` — "Export SLEAP File By Cam": camera×session grid.
  Each camera column exports across all its selected sessions into one SLEAP
  file; **Download All** bulk-exports every included column, **always
  prompting** for a destination folder (no cached `state.exportDirHandle`), and
  writes each included camera as a flat `<CamName>.slp` into it (falling back to
  per-file `downloadBlob` downloads without the File System Access API). A cell
  is a green ✓ (toggle on/off) only where the camera VIEW exists in that
  session — derived from `state.videoFiles` (real loaded views), plus cameras
  with labeled data for SLP-only projects; NOT from `session.cameras`, which is
  the full calibration list and would falsely imply existence. Sessions missing
  the view show a red ✗ (not selectable). The table **footer holds a per-column
  include toggle** (`.slp-bycam-incl`, ✓/✗) for Download All; a column whose
  toggled-on sessions have incompatible skeletons is **blocked** (toggle
  disabled with an explanatory `title`, excluded from the export) — checked
  set-based / order-insensitively via `findSkeletonMismatch` and re-evaluated on
  every cell toggle (`updateDownloadStates`), with a red warning under the
  tables (`#slpByCamSkelWarning`). The shared **Include** group's two options
  become the `instanceFilter` (`{user:true, predicted, reprojected}`) passed to
  `exportSlpMultiSession`. The Reprojections row is **disabled as a unit**
  (`.slp-inc-row-disabled`) unless at least one session has reprojections. Download All shows per-file
  progress; **Esc closes the modal**, or cancels an in-progress export mid-run.
  Columns ordered by session frequency, then within-session name order, then
  session recency for session-unique views.
- `showSlpExportAllModal()` — multi-session SLP export. **Deprecated**: not
  wired to a File-menu item; retained for reference.
- `showExport3DVideoModal()` — File ▸ "Export 3D Video". Mounts a second
  `Viewport3D` (reusing the panel code) in a modal so the user can orbit/zoom to
  pick the camera angle. Controls: a preview transport; **draggable start/end
  nodes** backed by **validated Start/End fields** (non-integer, out of
  `[0, lastFrame]`, or crossing the other bound is rejected and reverted); an
  editable FPS; a **`Quality` picker** — the four shared tiers 480p / 720p /
  1080p / 2160p.
  `V3D_RES` is **DERIVED from `RES_PRESETS`** in `ui/overlay-export-layout.js`
  (`refW` is the exact width here, since this viewport is always 16:9) and its
  H.264 level comes from that module's **`h264CodecFor`** — do not keep a
  private copy (a wrong level only surfaces mid-export at one resolution). Live
  readouts for **Output** (`W×H` + Mbps), **Duration**, **Exported Frames** and
  **Estimated File Size** — the shared **`estimatedBytes`**, the same number
  that decides whether the export streams to disk, so the readout and that
  decision cannot drift. Inputs are disabled during an export. Export renders
  only `[start, end]` at the chosen resolution (`renderer.setPixelRatio(1)` +
  `setSize(W,H)`), captures through an even-dimensioned 2D canvas, and encodes
  an `.mp4` through **`ui/video-encode.js`** (mediabunny), passing the H.264
  level as `fullCodecString`. When the
  estimate exceeds `shouldStreamToDisk`'s threshold it asks for a save-file
  destination and **streams** to it (declining cancels the export, since
  buffering was the thing being avoided); a short clip just downloads.
  Requires WebCodecs. Covered by `tests/e2e/export-3d-video.mjs`.
- `showTriangulateMultiFrameModal()` — frame-range triangulation modal
  (`triangulateMultiFrameInstances`); its average error comes from the
  returned `errSum` / `errN`, and its timeline pass walks `result.frames`.
  Its Start / End boxes show **1-based** frame numbers; Continue converts them
  to 0-based indices before validating and solving. It solves with Settings ▸ Default
  Triangulation (`resolveTriangulationMethod(null)` — the modal has no method
  picker) and names the method in its progress, summary and status text.
  Covered by `tests/e2e/triangulate-range-modal.mjs` (also Group by Track's
  3D-only-frame guard).
- `exportLabels()` — JSON labels export.
- `exportPoints3dH5()` — points3d H5 export.
- `exportReprojH5()` — reprojection H5 export.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./progress-delta.js` — `noteProgress` (the lit stretch on the Choose Frames for
  Triangulation and Export 3D Video bars).
- `./app-state.js` — `state`, `viewport3d`, `timeline`, `getActiveSession`.
- `./browser-hints.js` — `fileSystemAccessHint` (appended to the 3D-video and
  JSON-export "must be built in memory" confirms in Brave).
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `InstanceGroup`, `UnlinkedInstance`, `pooledPoints3d`
  (Group by Identity / Track & Triangulate All store each solve in the slab pool).
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `triangulateAndReproject`,
  `frameHasGroupedUserInstances`,
  `loadAllLazyFrames`, `triangulateMultiFrameInstances`,
  `sessionHasCalibration`, `showCalibrationRequiredPopup`,
  `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`, `sweepLazyFrameWindows`,
  `resolveTriangulationMethod`, `findEquivalentPriorGroup`, `adoptPrior3d`,
  `triangulationMethodLabel` (the last four for the adopt-don't-downgrade rule),
  `dropAllDerivedTriangulationState` (Group by Track keeps only the 3D).
- `./viewport3d.js` — `Viewport3D` (Export 3D Video modal).
- `./overlays.js` — `getTrackColor`, `getGroupColor` (Export 3D Video modal).
- `./rendering.js` — `drawAllOverlays`, `setReprojErrorVisible`,
  `showReprojectionsOnly`, `REPROJ_ONLY_NOTE` (both group-and-triangulate-all
  paths end Reproj-only).
- `./info-panel.js` — `updateInfoPanel`.
- `../pose/run-state.js` — `noteTriangulateRun` (after
  `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll`, Triangulate All's DLT path once
  identities exist, and `groupByTrackAndTriangulateAll`).
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `showLoading`, `hideLoading`,
  `setStatus`.
- `./loading-overlay.js` — `showLoadingProgress`, `yieldToPaint` (the
  "Grouping & triangulating" bar in both group-and-triangulate-all paths).
- `../pose/triangulation-pool.js` — `createGroupSolver`
  (`groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll` solves its non-adopted groups on the
  worker pool; adoption and regrouping stay on the main thread).
- `../import-export/file-io.js` — `exportSlpClientSide`,
  `exportSlpMultiSession`, `findSkeletonMismatch`, `buildPoints3dH5`,
  `buildReprojH5`.
- `../pose/initialization.js` — `update3DViewport`.
- `./video-encode.js` — `createMp4Writer`, `videoEncodingAvailable` (Export 3D
  Video).
- `./overlay-export-layout.js` — `RES_PRESETS`, `bitrateFor`, `estimatedBytes`,
  `h264CodecFor`, `shouldStreamToDisk` (the quality tiers shared with the
  overlay video export).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**User-facing features.** File menu Export (JSON / SLEAP File / SLEAP File By
Cam / **3D Video (.mp4)** / H5 points3d / H5 reproj), Edit menu Group-by-Track /
Group-by-Identity, Multi-Frame Triangulate modal.

---

### ui/identity-assignment.js

**Purpose.** All workflows for grouping instances into identities — manual
assignment, edit-group mode, track/identity helpers.

**Key exports.**
- Track helpers: `swapAssignTrack`, `assignTrackToSelected`,
  `propagateIdentityForward`, `assignIdentityToSelected`,
  `purgeTriangulationDataForGroup`, `swapTracks`.
  `swapTracks` and `swapAssignTrack` are **durable on a lazy project**: each
  writes the columnar store through the private `swapTracksInStore`
  (→ `SioLazyLoader.remapTracksFromIdentity`) and then mirrors the swap into
