  memory through `swapTracksInMemory`, which covers the resident `frameGroups`
  AND the project-wide `instanceGroups` placeholders under one `seen` set. See
  "A store write must be mirrored into `instanceGroups`" under
  `pose/triangulation.js` for why both maps are required and why the
  de-duplication is load-bearing. The reported count is the durable row count
  when a store exists, the in-memory changed count otherwise.
- Manual identity switch: `applyIdentitySwitch`, `describeIdentitySwitch`.
  **The single entry point for "the user picked a different ID for
  this instance"** — the `1`–`9` hotkeys (`assignIdentityToSelected`), the Linked
  Instance Groups identity dropdown and the unlinked-row identity dropdown
  (`ui/info-panel.js`) all route through it so they cannot drift. Three modes:
  - **swap** — the selection already reads as identity A and the user picks B, so
    A and B are EXCHANGED from the current frame to the end of the timeline in
    every view via `Session.swapIdentitiesForward`. A correction is a statement
    about the rest of the video, exactly as SLEAP's `track_swap` is for tracks.
    The private `resolveCurrentIdentityId` reads the CURRENT identity the way
    `getGroupColor` does — the per-frame entry for one of the selection's own live
    (camera, trackIdx) pairs first, `group.identityId` only as a fallback, because
    that field is stale on every frame but the last assignment's.
  - **propagate** — nothing to swap away from (fresh project / just-grouped group
    / "none" picked), so it falls back to the per-camera, per-track forward stamp
    (`assignIdentityToGroup` + `Session.propagateIdentity`), the only continuity
    signal available in that state.
  - **frame** — the unlinked row's instance is TRACKLESS (the optional
    `unlinkedInstance` argument, passed by both unlinked call sites, has
    `trackIdx == null`). `frameIdentityMap` cannot key a null track and no
    linkage exists to carry the correction to other frames, so this routes to
    `Session.assignIdentityToUnlinkedTrackless`: per-frame, instance-level,
    with an in-frame swap against the same camera's trackless row already
    holding the target identity. `describeIdentitySwitch` says "this frame"
    rather than implying propagation.

  The optional `scopeCamera` argument restricts either map mode to ONE camera,
  via `Session.swapIdentitiesForwardInCamera` in swap mode. Passed by the two
  UNGROUPED call sites — the unlinked-row dropdown in `ui/info-panel.js` and the
  `selectedUnlinked` branch of `assignIdentityToSelected` — and omitted by the
  group ones: an ungrouped instance is one 2D detection belonging to no
  cross-view bundle, so a correction on it speaks for that camera only (the
  ungroup → switch the wrong view → regroup workflow), while a GROUP asserts one
  animal across cameras. A scoped swap does not pin `group.identityId` (there is
  no group, and that field is shared by all views). The result carries `camera`
  so `describeIdentitySwitch` reports "cam2 only" instead of "all views".

  A per-raw-track `propagateIdentityForward` loop is NOT enough: it covers only
  one raw track and the cameras the group was visible in on that frame, so a
  switch stopped at the first track fragment. Pinned by
  `tests/e2e/identity-switch-propagates-to-end.mjs`. `describeIdentitySwitch`
  builds the status text so the reported count is what actually changed — a
  plausible count over a silently truncated range is how that bug hid. Still
  **forward-only**: earlier frames are never re-stamped, and
  `assignTrackToIdentity` (which re-stamped EVERY frame of a track) remains
  uncalled. Whole-track identity assignment is still available via
  **Tracks ▸ Propagate Tracks → IDs**.
- Manual assign: `manualAssignState`, `getTotalUnlinkedCount`,
  `cleanupManualAssignment`, `startManualAssignment`.
- Edit group: `editGroupState`, `startEditGroup`, `cancelEditGroup`,
  `finishEditGroup`, `cleanupEditGroup`, `updateEditGroupToast`.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./app-state.js` — `state`, `interactionManager`, `viewport3d`, `timeline`.
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `UnlinkedInstance`.
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`,
  `updateTimelineForFrame`.
- `./rendering.js` — `drawAllOverlays`.
- `./info-panel.js` — `updateInfoPanel`.
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `markDirty`, `setStatus`.

**No automatic assignment, and no `triangulateAndReproject` call.**
`tests/test-triangulation-method-propagation.mjs` requires that NO caller
hardcodes DLT. Cross-view grouping is Track All / Track Frame's job
(`pose/tracker.js`).

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`,
`ui/rendering.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**User-facing features.** Manual identity assignment toast workflow,
Edit Group mode, track-swap dialogs.

---

### ui/info-panel.js

**Purpose.** Right-hand info panel — populates the Videos, Cameras,
Skeleton, Sessions, and Frame Info tables; hosts the skeleton editor and
the per-frame instance-group / unlinked-instance tables. `updateInfoPanel` also
renders the Session tab's Recording section (`ui/recording-info.js`).

**Reprojection Error panel (`updateFrameInfo`).** The headline mean,
undistorted residual, method label, and per-camera rows are the frame SUMMARY
(averaged over every triangulated instance). The "Per-instance node breakdown"
`<details>` then renders ONE per-node × per-camera table PER instance, each
labelled with a color dot + track/identity (identity preferred, then track, then
`Instance N`) and that instance's own mean error, ordered by label. Reads each
`state.triangulationResults` entry's `{ group, errors, meanError }` — a shape
all three producers supply (`pose/triangulation.js`'s sweeps,
`import-export/save-load.js`'s load-time rebuild, and `ui/rendering.js`'s lazy
per-frame fill), so the panel populates for freshly-triangulated AND reopened
projects alike.
  **Label/color resolution reuses the canonical resolvers on purpose** — the
  panel must never disagree with what the same group shows elsewhere:
  - *Track*: the first member instance that actually CARRIES a track, scanning
    every camera (not `instances`' first entry, which can be a trackless view
    while its siblings are tracked) — the same scan `getGroupColor` and the
    identity `<select>` below use.
  - *Identity*: `session.getIdentityIdForTrack(cam, trackIdx, frameIdx)` FIRST,
    falling back to `group.identityId`, which is only refreshed on the frame an
    identity is (re)assigned. Mirrors the identity dropdown's pre-select exactly.
  - *Color*: `getGroupColor(group, session, state.colorByIdentity, frameIdx)`,
    so the dot matches the 2D views and 3D viewport — it honors
    Color-by-Identity mode and carries the wildcard-identity and frame-0
    trackIdx-collision guards that a bare `getTrackColor(trackIdx)` bypasses.
  - The synthetic "No ID" track (`isNoIdTrack`) is not shown as an animal name.
  **Only results for THIS frame's live groups are rendered.**
  `state.triangulationResults` is derived state that outlives the groups it
  describes: `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll` DELETES and rebuilds each
  frame's `instanceGroups` but never prunes the results map, and
  `ui/rendering.js`'s lazy fill CONCATENATES onto whatever is stored — so a
  frame can hold results for both the deleted groups and their replacements
  (two tables per animal). The breakdown filters `results` against the
  `instanceGroups` argument, keeping entries with no `group` (they use the
  `Instance N` fallback) and skipping the filter entirely when no group list
  was passed. Deliberately display-side, so the frame-summary headline still
  averages every stored entry.
  Labels are resolved ONCE up front against each result's ORIGINAL index (so the
  `Instance N` fallback numbering doesn't shuffle with the sort) and the original
  index breaks label ties, keeping several trackless groups in a stable order
  frame to frame. Covered by `tests/e2e/rpe-per-instance.mjs`.

**Instance tables layout: both fit a 300 px panel.** Grouped Instances is
Track / Identity · Views · Type · Error · unlink; Ungrouped Instances is
Track / Identity · Type · Points · Score / Node, under one full-width header
row per camera. Score / Node is STACKED like Track / Identity: the instance
score, and under it (muted, `.unlinked-node-score`) the instance's mean node
score (`meanNodeScore`, `pose/detection-filter-stats.js`: sleap-nn's, a missing
node counting as 0 — what the Tracking Wizard's "Min mean node score" filter
tests; "-" with no node scores, e.g. a user instance), explained on its header
(`SCORE_NODE_TIP`, `setInfoTip`). A fifth column did not fit: 273 px against
257 with the panel's scrollbar showing. The header is held at its narrowest
(`width: 1px`, "Score /" kept on one line) so "Track / Identity" stays on one. In both, a row's track and identity `<select>`s are STACKED in the
first cell, all `STACKED_SELECT_PX` (80) wide and left-aligned, track on top
(the Grouped row's dirty marker sits beside its track dropdown) — side by side
they made the Instances tab scroll sideways. The track dropdown is the FIRST
select in that cell — tests and code that look for it must say so
(`select:first-of-type`).

Under each group that has reprojections, `updateFrameInfo` adds a row:
a REPROJECTION_COLOR dot and a "Reprojection" badge (`.badge-reproj`) in the
Track / Identity cell, titled "Reprojection of <name>"; the
reprojected view count (`n/cameras`) under Views; an EMPTY Type cell; the Error
dash; and the trailing empty cell — one cell per header column. The badge leads
the row rather than sitting under Type so the Type column only has to fit
"Pred*", which is what makes the width above possible. Covered by
`tests/e2e/info-panel-instance-tables.mjs`, which measures each table's fit as
its MIN-CONTENT width (the tables are `width: 100%`, so their rendered width
always equals their container and cannot show an overflow).

**Instance-panel track/identity dropdowns.** Each grouped/unlinked instance
row has a track `<select>` and an identity `<select>`. Both selects include a
`(none)` option (value `-1`) and a `(+) New Track` / `(+) New Identity` option (value
`__new__`). The track select defaults to `(none)` for a trackless instance/group
(trackIdx == null) — it does NOT snap to the first track (index 0); selecting
`(none)` sets the instance(s) trackless (the group path also unassigns its
identity). Choosing `(+) New …` replaces the select with an inline text box
(`startInlineNameEntry`) where the user types a name and presses Enter to create
+ assign it (Esc or blur cancels); tracks are deduped by name, identities reuse
an existing same-named identity. These assignment/create handlers (and the
`assign*ToSelected` helpers in `ui/identity-assignment.js`) refresh the
timeline with `{ keepSize: true }` so a track/identity edit never regrows the
bottom timeline panel.

**The Track `<select>` is built LAZILY** (`buildTrackSelect`, via
`ui/lazy-select.js`). Until the user presses or focuses it, it holds three
options — the head (`(none)`), the current track and `(+) New Track` —
and the full list is filled in on that first `mousedown` / `focus`, both of
which fire before the browser opens the list or acts on a key. An eager select
per row on every update, with hundreds of tracks, capped playback at ~5 fps
(`tests/e2e/_bench-playback.mjs`). What a closed select shows is unchanged
except its closed WIDTH (capped by `max-width` either way). The **Identity**
selects stay eager: one option per animal, never part of the cost, and a lazy
select ignores a scripted `.value` until focused, which
`tests/e2e/ungroup-retains-identity.mjs` and `ungroup-trackless-reopen.mjs`
rely on. Covered by `tests/test-lazy-select.js` and
`tests/e2e/info-panel-many-tracks.mjs`.

**The panel tab bar is ONE horizontal scroller.** `setupPanelTabs` makes
`.panel-tabs` scroll sideways with every tab (Instances, Visibility, ID
Switches, Videos, Cameras, Skeleton, Session) always inside it, in markup
order, each sized to its full name. Do not bring back a "More ▾" overflow
dropdown: it hid most tabs at the default width, and which ones moved as the
panel was resized.
- **A vertical wheel scrolls it too** (a trackpad swipe over the strip is
  vertical); the bar sits OUTSIDE `.panel-tab-content`, so this breaks no
  "one scroller" rule. It `preventDefault()`s only when the bar moved, so a
  swipe past either end is still the page's.
- **Click-and-drag has a 4px threshold, and a real drag SUPPRESSES the click**
  (a capture-phase `click` listener on the bar, cleared on the next
  `pointerdown`). Pointer capture is taken only once the threshold is crossed —
  capturing on every press re-targets the plain click that follows. Touch
  pointers are skipped: native `pan-x` already pans, and driving `scrollLeft`
  on top would move it twice.
- **Selecting a tab scrolls it into view**, by hand rather than with
  `scrollIntoView` (which could scroll the app's layout), so
  `openIdSwitchPanel` (`ui/id-switch-modal.js`, which just `.click()`s the
  button) never leaves the active tab off-screen. `.panel-tabs` is
  `position: relative` so each tab's `offsetLeft` is in the scroller's space.
- **The edge fades are the only affordance** (`scroll-left` / `scroll-right`
  classes, drawn with `mask-image`; the scrollbar is suppressed), recomputed by
  a `ResizeObserver`.
- **`user-select: none` on the bar** — the documented exception for "a surface
  whose job is to be dragged".
Covered by `tests/e2e/panel-tabs-scroller.mjs`.

**The Videos tab's two buttons are wired at SETUP, not per-session.**
`setupVideosTab()` (called once from `pose/initialization.js`) installs the
`#btnLoadVideos` → `handleLoadVideos` and `#btnRemoveVideo` → `removeVideoFile`
handlers. Assigned inside `updateInfoPanel`, which returns early when
`state.session` is null (and while the panel is collapsed), **Load Videos**
would have no handler on a freshly-opened app.
- The buttons are **`Load Videos`** (named after the menu item it duplicates)
  and **`Remove Video`** (singular — it acts on the one selected row).
- The selected row is held by **identity** (`selectedVideoFile`), not by row
  index: both buttons rebuild the table, and an index into the OLD table names
  a different video in the new one. `populateVideosTable` clears the selection,
  disables `Remove Video` and resets the detail block on every rebuild.
Covered by `tests/e2e/videos-panel-buttons.mjs`.

**Key exports.**
- Tab control: `setupPanelTabs`, `setupVideosTab`.
- Tables: `populateVideosTable`, `populateCamerasTable`,
  `populateSkeletonTable`, `populateSessionAssignTable`,
  `populateUnassignedVideos`.
- Detail dialogs: `showVideoFileDetail`, `showCameraDetail`.
- Skeleton editor: `setupSkeletonEditing`, `exportSkeletonJSON` (download wrapper
  around `buildSkeletonJSON`). `parseSkeletonJSON` lives in
  `import-export/skeleton-json.js`.
- Project skeleton: `applyProjectSkeleton(sk, onDone)` (replace every session's
  skeleton via `setProjectSkeleton`, with the warn-on-overwrite modal) and
  `promptImportSkeletonForAllSessions` (the multi-session load's skeleton
  prompt, whose `onDone` runs the calibration comparison).
- Per-frame data: `updateInfoPanel`, `updateFrameInfo`,
  `updateTriangulationBadge`.
- Visibility tab: `populateTimelineVisibility(session)` (see below).
- Session: `ensureSession` (seeds new sessions from `buildRememberedSkeleton`).

**Collapsed panel does nothing.** `updateInfoPanel` and `updateFrameInfo` both
stop early when `isInfoPanelVisible()` (`ui/panel-visibility.js`) is false,
marking the panel stale so `refreshInfoPanelAfterShow` (`ui-wiring.js`) rebuilds
it once on re-show. Not a micro-optimization: `updateFrameInfo` runs on every
frame (throttled to 10 Hz during playback) and rebuilds the breakdown plus both
instance tables, and `updateInfoPanel` calls `populateVideosTable`, which walks
EVERY frame of the session per video row. Three things deliberately stay
outside the gate:
- **The status bar**, which is NOT part of `#infoPanel` and is always on
  screen. `updateFrameInfo`'s tail writes `#statusError` and calls
  `updateFrameCounters()`, so gating the whole function would silently freeze
  the bottom bar. Two private helpers split it: `aggregateReprojectionError`
  (the three summary numbers, cheap) runs first and unconditionally, then
  `updateStatusBarForFrame` runs on both the hidden and the visible path.
  `updateInfoPanel`'s hidden branch calls `updateFrameInfo` for the same
  reason. `updateStatusBarForFrame` does **not** call `updateFrameCounters()`
  while `state.isPlaying`: the counters are independent of the current frame,
  and recomputing them on the 10 Hz playback updates dropped video frames.
  `VideoController.stopPlayback` redraws with `isPlaying` false, which
  refreshes them on stop.
- The reprojection **solve** in `ui/rendering.js`'s per-frame fill. The canvas
  overlays, the 3D viewport and the `.slp` export all read the same
  `_grp.reprojections` / `reprojectedInstances` it produces, and the fill only
  fires once per group — skipping it for a hidden panel would blank the canvas
  markers and permanently starve the panel of numbers it could never recompute.
  Only the panel's *consumption* of `state.triangulationResults` is skippable.
- `rememberSkeleton(state.session.skeleton)`, which the gated branch still
  calls. It is `populateSkeletonTable`'s one non-DOM side effect, and
  `buildRememberedSkeleton()` feeds `ensureSession` — a skeleton loaded while
  the panel was hidden must not leave the next new session with a stale one.

**Collapsible Skeleton sections.** The Skeleton tab's **Nodes** and **Edges** are
`<details class="info-section info-collapsible">` (`index.html`), so either table
can be folded away. Native `<details>`/`<summary>`, which
`ui/keyboard-target.js` treats as owning Space/Enter when focused, so toggling
cannot fall through to the transport's Space shortcut. `populateSkeletonTable` keeps the
`#skeletonNodesCount` / `#skeletonEdgesCount` summary badges in sync;
`import-export/save-load.js`'s clear-project path resets them to `0` alongside
the tbodies. `setupSkeletonEditing` wires `persistSectionState` on both, under
`localStorage.skeletonSectionsOpen` — browser-local display taste, never
project state, so it does NOT go in the `.slp`.

**Every skeleton edit confirms first, and marks the project dirty.** All five
node/edge controls (add node, the `×` on a node row, the node-name field's
`change`, add edge, the `×` on an edge row) run their mutation inside a
`confirmSkeletonEdit` callback (`ui/skeleton-edit-warning.js`) and call
`markDirty()`. Details:
- **The rename handler puts the typed text back** (`nameInput.value =
  sk.nodes[i]`) right after raising the dialog, and the confirm callback's
  `populateSkeletonTable()` rebuilds the row with the new name. Otherwise a
  cancelled rename leaves the field showing a name the skeleton does not have.
- **Add Edge validates BEFORE confirming.** A duplicate or self-edge is a no-op,
  and asking the user to approve something that then does nothing makes the
  dialog read as noise. The `addEdge` call inside the callback keeps its own
  guard, since the dialog is dismissible and the model can move under it.
- **`propagateNodeAddedToSessions` passes `{hidden: true}`** — this is the
  INTERACTIVE path, so the new node has to be reachable. See
  `Session.propagateNodeAdded`.
- **The callback's `sessions` argument is the SCOPE, and the handlers honour
  it.** `confirmSkeletonEdit` settles which sessions are on this skeleton
  before the mutation and passes them in. `adoptEditedSkeleton(sk, sessions)`
  re-points exactly those and remembers `sk` as the project default;
  `propagateNodeAddedToSessions` / `propagateNodeRemovedFromSessions` re-shape
  exactly those. Deliberately NOT `setProjectSkeleton`, which re-points every
  loaded session — a session on a differently-shaped skeleton is excluded from
  the dialog's counts and named separately, so adopting it here would discard
  the skeleton it was loaded with. The two whole-project paths,
  `applyProjectSkeleton` and the Load Skeleton file reader, still call
  `setProjectSkeleton`: replacing every session's skeleton is what they are
  for.
- **`dropDerivedTriangulationResults()` runs after both node propagations.**
  `state.triangulationResults` (and each session's own copy) is a per-frame
  snapshot of the last solve at the OLD node count; `ui/rendering.js`'s fill
  concatenates onto whatever is stored, so a surviving entry makes the
  per-instance breakdown list every animal twice. Node edits only — a rename or
  an edge edit moves no coordinates.
- **`#skeletonWarnOffNote` is the way back from "Do not show again."**
  `populateSkeletonTable` sets its `display` from
  `isSkeletonEditWarningSuppressed()`, and `setupSkeletonEditing` wires
  `#btnSkeletonWarnOn` at SETUP — like the Videos tab's two buttons and for the
  same reason: a handler assigned inside a per-session rebuild is missing
  exactly when there is no session yet.

**Skeleton persistence.** `populateSkeletonTable` calls `rememberSkeleton` on every
refresh, so `ensureSession` (and the session-loader fresh-session sites) seed
new sessions with the current skeleton via `buildRememberedSkeleton`.
In-memory only; see `ui/app-state.js`.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `Skeleton`, `Camera`, `Session`.
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`.
- `../pose/detection-filter-stats.js` — `meanNodeScore` (the Ungrouped table's
  mean node score, under the instance score).
- `./info-tip.js` — `setInfoTip` (the Score / Node header's explanation).
- `./overlays.js` — `REPROJECTION_COLOR`, `NULL_ID_COLOR`, `getTrackColor`,
  `getGroupColor`.
- `./rendering.js` — `drawAllOverlays`, `updateFrameCounters`.
- `./timeline-visibility.js` — `toggle{Camera,Track,Identity}Visibility`,
  `get{Camera,Track,Identity}VisibilityList` (the Visibility tab's Timeline
  toggles).
- `./interaction.js` — `isInteractiveClickTarget`.
- `./panel-visibility.js` — `isInfoPanelVisible`, `markInfoPanelStale`.
- `./section-state.js` — `persistSectionState` (Skeleton ▸ Nodes / Edges,
  under the `skeletonSectionsOpen` key).
- `./lazy-select.js` — `buildLazySelect`, behind `buildTrackSelect` (both
  instance tables' Track `<select>`s).
- `./skeleton-edit-warning.js` — `confirmSkeletonEdit`, wrapped around all five
  node/edge mutations, plus `isSkeletonEditWarningSuppressed` /
  `setSkeletonEditWarningSuppressed` for `#skeletonWarnOffNote`.
- `./id-switch-modal.js` — `refreshIdSwitchPanel`: `updateInfoPanel` re-renders
  the ID Switches tab (and its seekbar markers) for the active session.
- `./app-state.js` — `state`, `timeline`, `interactionManager`,
  `rememberSkeleton`, `buildRememberedSkeleton`, `setProjectSkeleton`,
  `getProjectSkeleton`.
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `setStatus`, `markDirty`.
- `../import-export/skeleton-json.js` — `buildSkeletonJSON`, `parseSkeletonJSON`.
- `../loading/session-loader.js` — `handleLoadVideos`,
  `handleLoadCalibration`, `autoAssignVideosToCameras`,
  `createViewForVideoFile`, `rebuildVideoController`,
  `fitCanvasesToCells`, `loadSingleSessionFromCache`, `removeVideoFile`.
- `./ui-wiring.js` — `unlinkGroup`, `showGroupContextMenu`.
- `./identity-assignment.js` — `swapAssignTrack`, `applyIdentitySwitch`,
  `describeIdentitySwitch`. Both identity `<select>`s (the Linked Instance
  Groups row and the unlinked row) drive their change through
  `applyIdentitySwitch` and report via `describeIdentitySwitch`. They differ in
  SCOPE: the grouped row switches every view, while the **unlinked row passes
  its own camera as `scopeCamera`** (the ungroup → fix one view → regroup
  workflow) and passes its instance so a TRACKLESS row takes the per-frame
  instance-level path (mode **frame**; picking "(none)" on a trackless row
  clears `Instance.identityId` directly, there being no map entry to clear).
  The unlinked row's ID `<select>` pre-selects from
  `getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance` (per-frame map entry for a tracked row,
  instance-level retained identity for a trackless one), which is why
  `Session.unlinkGroup` has to retain the disbanded group's identity in the map
  / on the instance for the row to read as anything but "(none)".
- `./sessions-panes.js` — `populateSessionsPanel`, `populateViewStrip`,
  `populateSessionStrip`.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `pose/tracker.js`,
`pose/triangulation.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `loading/session-loader.js`,
`ui/rendering.js`, `ui/identity-assignment.js`, `ui/export-modals.js`,
`ui/sessions-panes.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**User-facing features.** All right-panel tabs (Videos, Cameras, Skeleton,
Sessions, Frame Info), skeleton editor (add/remove nodes, edges,
import/export JSON), per-frame instance-group context menus,
triangulation status badge.

**Visibility tab — Timeline subsection.** `populateTimelineVisibility(session)`
(exported) plus a private `buildVisToggleRow(entry, onChange, opts)` helper
that renders one `.toggle-switch` row inside `#visTimelineCameras` /
`#visTimelineTracks` / `#visTimelineIdentities`. Track AND identity rows both
render a `.vis-color-swatch`: identity rows from `identity.color`, track rows
from `getTrackColor(i, state.colorByIdentity)` where `i` is the row's position
in `session.tracks` — the same palette-index AND palette the timeline canvas
uses for the bar (`NULL_ID_COLOR` for the No ID track). The Tracks / Identity
change handler (`ui/ui-wiring.js`) re-runs `populateTimelineVisibility` so the
swatches switch palette with the mode. Camera rows have no swatch.

The change listener calls `toggle{Camera,Track,Identity}Visibility(session, name)`
followed by `timeline.refreshTracks(session, { keepSize: true })` (rebuild and
repaint without resizing — see `ui/timeline.js`'s `refreshTracks`), then
re-renders the toggle lists. `populateTimelineVisibility` is called from
`updateInfoPanel(...)` and again from `switchSession` after
`timeline.setData(newSession)` so the lists reflect the freshly-active
session's hidden sets.

**Visibility tab — section order + Display Legend.** In `index.html` the
**Timeline** subsection is at the top of the Visibility panel; **Display
Legend** is its own `<h3>` section between Reprojections and Video Brightness.
Every static checkbox in the panel uses the `.toggle-switch` markup.


---

### ui/interaction.js

**Purpose.** Mouse and keyboard interaction system — node selection,
dragging, hit testing, instance conversion, manual-assignment selection,
edit-group mode, keyboard shortcuts.

**Key exports.**
- `InteractionManager` — class wired by `pose/initialization.js`. Selected
  methods: `attach(views)`, `detach()`, `select`, `clearSelection`,
  `findNearestNode`, `findNearestUnlinkedNode`, `setAssignmentMode`,
  `setEditGroupMode`, `addToAssignmentSelection`,
  `getAssignmentSelectedIds`, `onMouseDown`/`onMouseMove`/`onMouseUp`/
  `onMouseLeave`/`onWheel`, `onKeyDown`, `_addNewInstance` (used by smart-add;
  lays out a new skeleton via an inline BFS fan-out from the highest-degree
  root, with a vertical-line fallback when there are no edges).
  `canvasToVideo(clientX, clientY, viewName)` maps a pointer to video pixels;
  on a ROTATED view it must use `zoom.scaleY` for the vertical axis (it differs
  a hair from `zoom.scale` at native resolution), or a click lands up to ~0.3%
  of the frame height off.
- `isInteractiveClickTarget(target)` — used by other UI to skip
  click-through on form controls.

**Plane annotation (View ▸ Define Planes).** A `PlaneInstance`
(`pose/plane-data.js`) is edited through the SAME code path as a UserInstance —
`hasPoint`/`getX`/`getY`/`setPoint`/`numNodes` is the whole interface the drag
path needs, and PlaneInstance inherits all of it — so plane editing cannot
drift from pose editing. Added surface:
- `findNearestPlaneNode(vx, vy, viewName)` — mirrors `findNearestNode`
  (nodes, then edges; edge hit → `nodeIdx: -1`), same zoom-corrected
  thresholds, but only over the indices `getPlaneNodeIndices(viewName)` reports
  as SHOWN (also passed as `_resolveNearestNode`'s `allowed`): a plane instance
  covers the whole node pool, so unfiltered, a node not placed on this view
  would take a click while drawing nothing.
- `selectPlane(plane, nodeIdx)` + `selectedPlane` / `selectedPlaneNodeIdx` /
  `hoveredPlaneNode`. Plane and pose selections are **mutually exclusive**
  (each `select*` clears the other); `clearSelection()` clears both.
- The `beginPlaneDrag(viewName, nodeIdx, wholePlane)` callback, asked once at
  mousedown. `allowed:false` refuses the drag (a PINNED node) while the click
  still SELECTS, so the node stays reachable for un-pinning. Its `indices`
  become `dragInfo.indexFilter`, honoured by `_onDragMove`/`_onDragUp` in
  whole-instance mode, so an Alt+drag moves only the grabbed plane's nodes.
- The `isPlaneDataLocked()` callback, asked before a right-click nulls a plane
  node (a geometry edit, like a drag). True while something reads plane
  geometry live (the Set Angle dialog). Deliberately NOT folded into
  `isPlaneEditMode`, which also gates hit-testing — with that false a click
  meant for a plane corner would grab a pose node. Locked plane nodes still
  select and hover. Both refusing callbacks put the reason in the status bar
  themselves; this module cannot explain a refusal it does not own.
- `onPlaneChanged(planeInstance, movedIndices, {moved})` — `movedIndices` is
  the touched node indices (`[nodeIdx]`, the alt-drag filter, or null =
  unknown), so only those nodes' 3D is invalidated. `moved` is `true` for a
  DRAG, `false` for a right-click null-toggle: only a drag promotes a
  REPROJECTED corner to a real observation.
- `_startDrag`'s trailing `plane` argument (`dragInfo.plane`): planes live
  outside `frameGroups`, so they are held by reference rather than re-resolved
  mid-drag. `onMouseUp` skips the `instance.type = 'user'` promotion for a
  plane — it must not enter the pose pipeline's vocabulary.
- Gestures: click to select, drag a node, **Alt+drag** to translate the whole
  plane (reusing `altDragSource`'s `mode: 'instance'`), **right-click** to
  toggle a node off into `nulledNodes`.
- The node hit radius comes from the `getPlaneNodeSize` callback (the shared
  Plane Appearance ▸ Node Size slider), so what you can grab is always what you
  can see; `planeHitRadius` is the fallback when no callback is supplied.

**Everything plane-related is gated on `_planeEditable()`** — the
`isPlaneEditMode` + `getPlaneInstances` callbacks. Outside "Defining Plane
Mode" every plane branch is dead, so plane nodes can never compete with pose
nodes for a click during normal annotation; with no callbacks wired the whole
feature is inert. Inside the mode, planes are checked FIRST on mousedown/hover
so a plane node on top of a pose instance is still grabbable, and a MISS falls
through to the normal handling so pose editing keeps working.
`tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs` pins both directions of that gate.
**Deselecting an unlinked instance clears the Delete target too.**
`assignmentSelection` (the amber ring / Ungrouped Instances highlight) and
`selectedUnlinked` (what `_deleteSelected` acts on) both track an unlinked
selection. `addToAssignmentSelection`'s toggle-off branch (a plain re-click,
via `_unlinkedWasSelected`) also clears `selectedUnlinked` when it names the
same instance, so nothing is armed for **Delete** without a ring saying so.
`drawUnlinkedInstances`' `selectedUnlinkedId` stays deliberately undrawn (the
amber ring already marks it); it is kept as the hook for a PRIMARY selection
within a multi-camera one. Tests: `tests/test-assignment.js` "Assignment -
deselect clears the Delete target too".

**Alt + wheel rotates a whole instance about the node under the cursor**
(issue #198), ported from SLEAP's `QtNode.wheelEvent`
(`sleap/gui/widgets/video.py`): **6° per wheel notch**, clockwise on scroll up.
One `_applyInstanceTransform` does the rigid rotate-then-translate for every
path, always from a points snapshot taken at gesture start, so a burst cannot
accumulate drift and rotation composes with a simultaneous drag.

Two entry points:
- **Button held (SLEAP's gesture).** Alt+press starts the whole-instance drag;
  `_onDragWheel` (document-level, capture phase, so it beats wheel-to-zoom)
  turns it about `dragInfo.pivot`. A pure rotation never clears the drag
  deadzone, so `onMouseUp` treats non-zero `rotationDeg` as a change.
- **No button (LUCID addition, for macOS trackpads).** `onWheel` handles
  Alt+wheel while hovering a node. The gesture latches in `_rotateGesture` and
  commits on ~200 ms idle (`_scheduleRotateCommit`/`_commitRotateGesture`), the
  next click, or `detach()` — so `onNodeMoved` runs once per burst, as a drag
  commits once on release.

**Label text does not turn with the skeleton**: node names and the
track/identity pill are drawn inside `ui/overlays.js`'s `beginUprightFrame`,
which cancels only the VIEW's rotation; only label *placement*
(`computeLabelOffset`) follows the turned skeleton. Pinned by the `Instance
Rotate - node names stay horizontal` tests.

`wheelNotches` folds `deltaMode` in and **ignores `deltaX`** (unlike SLEAP,
which sums x and y; on a trackpad the horizontal component fights the vertical).

**Holding Alt suspends wheel-to-zoom outright.** An Alt+wheel is ALWAYS consumed
(`_consumeWheel`), even over empty canvas or a reprojection, so a scroll that
strays off the skeleton mid-rotation cannot yank the view. TWO guards, each
pinned separately:
- `onWheel`'s `_consumeWheel`, for a wheel ON an overlay canvas.
- `loading/video.js`'s wheel handler returning early on `e.altKey` — the only
  guard for a `.video-cell`'s **letterbox margin**, where no overlay canvas is
  under the cursor.

Releasing Alt restores zoom. Within a gesture the rotation IS latched: a stray
Alt+wheel off-skeleton keeps turning the same instance about the same pivot.

Coverage: `tests/test-instance-rotate.js` and
`tests/e2e/alt-wheel-rotate-instance.mjs` (rotation and zoom never both fire,
incl. the letterbox margin).

**Zoom-aware thresholds.** `_displayToVideo(state, viewName)` returns how many
video pixels span one CSS pixel on screen given the view's current `zoom.scale`.
Hit-test padding (`findNearestNode`/`findNearestUnlinkedNode`) and the drag-start
deadzone (`_onDragMove`, ~3 CSS px) multiply by it so they stay constant on screen
(a fixed video-px deadzone would force a large on-screen drag at high zoom).

**A delete is a STORE delete first (`_deleteSelected`).** The Delete key, Edit ▸
Delete Instance and the toolbar's "- Instance" all land in `_deleteSelected`,
which calls `deleteTargetsFromStore` (`ui/custom-delete-ops.js`) with the
selected ungrouped instance (`unlinkedTarget`) or the deleted group members
(`groupMemberTargets` — one view, or every view for Shift+Delete) BEFORE it
edits the frame. On a lazy project a resident-only delete would not survive
(re-hydration and the streaming save both read the store). The store call goes
first because it identifies victims by `_rawInstIndex` while they are still in
the frame and renumbers survivors. An ungrouped delete's camera is
`ul.cameraName`, not `lastInteractedView`. Eager sessions have no store; only
the in-memory edit applies. Covered by
`tests/test-custom-delete-ops.js` ("interactive deletes reach the store") and
`tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs` cycle 5c.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `Instance`.
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `getOrComputeReprojectedInstance`.
- `./keyboard-target.js` — `shouldIgnoreShortcut`.
- `./custom-delete-ops.js` — `deleteTargetsFromStore`, `groupMemberTargets`,
  `unlinkedTarget` (import-free itself, so this adds no cycle).
- `./node-tooltip.js` — `nodeTooltipText`, `setNodeTooltipTarget`,
  `hideNodeTooltip`.
- `./overlays.js` — `getGroupColor`, `unlinkedDupIndices`, `unlinkedBaseColor`
  (the node tooltip's color; `overlays.js` imports nothing, so no cycle).

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`.

**The node tooltip is fed from the hover.** `onMouseMove` hands
`_nodeTooltipTarget(state, view, frame, hit, ulHit)` to
`setNodeTooltipTarget` on every move: the hovered node's name, its
`Session.predictedNodeScore` (no tooltip on an edge, plane node or empty
canvas; no score on a reprojection), and the instance's drawn color
(`getGroupColor` / `unlinkedBaseColor`, never computed separately). It reports
the instance a click would select (grouped wins over ungrouped).
`onMouseLeave` hides it. See `ui/node-tooltip.js`.

**User-facing features.** Click-to-select skeleton nodes, drag to move
keypoints, Alt+drag to move a whole instance, Alt+wheel to rotate one about a
node, double-click to convert predicted → user, shift-drag to add
to manual-assignment selection, right-click to null/restore nodes,
keyboard shortcuts (delete, alt-drag clone, etc.).

**`onKeyDown` guards with `shouldIgnoreShortcut`** (`ui/keyboard-target.js`)
rather than its own `tagName === 'INPUT'` test, so `Delete` / `n` / `c` survive
a click on a checkbox (issue #163).

**Grouping/ungrouping shortcuts.** `onKeyDown` handles only the legacy `c`
confirm-group alias (creates a group from a ready ≥2 assignment selection).
The primary group (`Shift+G`) and ungroup (`Shift+U`) shortcuts are
**catalog-dispatched** and wired in `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`setHandler`); ungroup
delegates to that module's `unlinkGroup` (the complete path: data-model
`Session.unlinkGroup` + triangulation purge + overlay/3D/timeline/info-panel
refresh).

---

### ui/keyboard-target.js

**Purpose.** Decide whether a keystroke belongs to the focused control or to the
app — the single guard every global `keydown` handler calls (issue #163).

A plain `tagName === 'INPUT'` test is wrong: a **checkbox** (radio, slider,
file picker) is an `INPUT` too, so clicking one would kill every shortcut.

**The two halves.**
1. *Ask what the control consumes, not what tag it is.* A text field blocks
   everything; a checkbox owns exactly `Space`; a range slider the arrows and
   `Home`/`End`; a button `Space` and `Enter`. Everything else stays live.
2. *Give focus back after a POINTER click* (`installFocusRelease`), since a
   focused checkbox genuinely owns `Space` and play/pause would stay broken.
   **Keyboard focus is deliberately left alone** (`:focus-visible`): tab to a
   checkbox, press `Space`, still get a toggle.

**The third owner: the browser.** The catalog dispatcher `preventDefault()`s
`Mod+C` (Copy selected instance), which also cancels the browser's copy.
`shouldIgnoreShortcut` hands the copy/cut chord back **whenever a text
selection exists**, and only then. **Paste deliberately is not** included —
having text selected says nothing about wanting to replace it.

**Key exports.**
- `shouldIgnoreShortcut(e)` — the guard itself: the clipboard-chord rule above,
  then `isTextEntryTarget(e.target) || targetOwnsKey(e.target, e)`.
- `hasTextSelection(doc)` — true for a non-collapsed selection with text in it.
  The whole read is wrapped: a selection inside a shadow root or a cross-origin
  frame can throw, and the honest answer there is "none I can speak for".
- `isTextEntryTarget(t)` — free-text targets, which block every shortcut:
  `<textarea>`, `contenteditable`, the text-ish `<input>` types, `role=textbox`
  /`searchbox`/`combobox`, **and `<select>`** (arrows, `Enter` and letter
  typeahead are nearly the whole shortcut alphabet). An `<input>` with a missing
  or unknown type counts as `text`, the safe default.
- `targetOwnsKey(t, e)` — whether THIS key is the focused control's own. A
  modifier chord never is, so `Mod+S` still saves while a checkbox has focus.
- `isTransientFocusControl(t)` / `releaseTransientFocus(el, doc)` /
  `installFocusRelease(doc)` — the focus-release half. Text fields, selects and
  sliders are never released: focus there is the beginning of an interaction,
  not the end of one.

`:focus-visible`, not `event.detail`, separates pointer from keyboard: a
`<label>` click forwards a **synthetic** click with `detail: 0`, and every
toolbar checkbox in `index.html` is wrapped in a label.

The `blur()` is deferred a turn: focus from a label click lands *after* the
listener runs, and `input`/`change` are still dispatching.

The listener is in the **capture** phase (issue #230) so a control whose own
handler calls `e.stopPropagation()` (the toolbar's action menus,
`wireActionMenu`) cannot opt itself out of focus release and keep `Space`.

**Imports from project modules.** None — every predicate reads only
`tagName` / `type` / `role` / `isContentEditable` off its argument, so it
bridges into both test runners and unit-tests against plain object stubs.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/settings.js`, `ui/interaction.js`,
`loading/video.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-keyboard-target.js`,
`tests/e2e/checkbox-focus-hotkeys.mjs`, `tests/e2e/copy-panel-text.mjs` (the
clipboard rule) and `tests/e2e/triangulate-button-focus.mjs` (the capture
phase, incl. a synthesized stop-propagation button).

---

### ui/loading-progress-modal.js

**Purpose.** Generic per-task progress panel for long-running load
operations. Designed to be plugged into video decoder loads (per-camera
rows) and future SLP project parsing. Per-row weighted-monotonic bar
(canplay × 0.1 + mp4box × 0.9) prevents reset at the phase boundary;
phase color flips signal transitions (red → blue → green).

**Key exports.**
- `LoadingProgressModal` (class) — flat task API: `addTask`, `updateTask`,
  `completeTask`, `failTask`, `show`, `dismiss`, `reset`, `isOpen`,
  `getTaskState`. Two-level (session-group + child task) API:
  `addSessionGroup({ label })` (alias: `addSession`, `addParentTask`) →
  `groupId`; `addTaskToSession(groupId, { label })` (alias: `addChildTask`);
  `setCurrentSession(groupId)` (alias: `setActiveSession`);
  `completeSession(groupId)` (alias: `finishSession`);
  `failSession(groupId, error)`; `setProjectImportHeader({ current, total })`
  (alias: `setHeader`, `setSessionProgress`). `addTask({ sessionId })`
  attaches a flat-API task as a child of the named group. Header format:
  `${title} - Session ${current} of ${total}`. Constructor takes
  `{ title, autoDismissMs, minVisibleMs }`.
- `getLoadingProgressModal(options)` — module-level lazy singleton.
  Refreshes `_singleton.title` and the header on each call, so the first
  caller's title does not stick.
- `resetLoadingProgressModal()` — test-only helper to drop the singleton.

**Imports from project modules.** `./progress-delta.js` (`noteProgress`, each
bar's lit stretch in `_renderRow`; import-free).

**Imported by.** `ui/sessions-panes.js` (switchSession), `loading/session-loader.js`
(handleLoadVideos), `import-export/save-load.js` (handleLoadProject V3 path),
`import-export/slp-import.js` (handleLoadSlpFile per-cam loop).

**User-facing features.** Bottom-right per-camera progress rows during
session switching and initial-load workflows. Auto-dismisses ~500 ms
after all tasks complete; stays open on error.

**Notes / caveats.**
- `root` is the live DOM and the only rendering: rows and groups are built
  with `createElement`/`appendChild` and updated in place.
- Long session names truncate with ellipsis at the modal max-width (380 px).
  Its CSS is injected by the module itself, not in `styles.css`.

---

### ui/progress-delta.js

**Purpose.** Every progress bar lights up the stretch it gained on its last
update: a `.progress-delta` element in the bar's track, from the previous
fraction to the new one, drawn over the fill as a translucent white tint (a
brighter shade of whatever colour the bar is, no colour per bar) with a 700 ms
white flash as it appears (an opacity animation on `::after`, so it runs on the
compositor). It stays lit until the next update replaces it, so a glance says
how far the bar moved on its last step — the way a game's health bar lights up
the chunk a hit took off. It grows with the fill's OWN transition (duration and
timing function of the fill's `width` / `transform` transition, read from its
computed style), so its leading edge moves with the fill's instead of running
ahead of it. Moving back, a snap (a new stage, a reset) or the first update of
a bar show nothing. `prefers-reduced-motion` drops the flash, not the tint.
Styles: `.progress-delta` in `styles.css` (by the loading overlay's bar).

**Key exports.**
- `noteProgress(fill, frac, { animate })` — call right after setting a bar's
  fill to `frac` (0..1). Remembers the fraction on the fill element; `animate:
  false` means the fill snapped, so no stretch is lit. Works for a `width`
  fill and for a full-width fill scaled with `transform: scaleX` (the loading
  overlay's). A static track is given `position: relative` once, for the
  absolutely placed tint. A no-op without a real DOM (Node test stubs, stand-in
  elements).
- `resetProgress(fill)` — forget the last fraction (a bar reused from zero).

**Callers (every progress bar).** `ui/loading-overlay.js` (`_setFill`: Track
All, Triangulate All, Save, loading, the body-size check, …), `ui/id-switch-modal.js`
(the image ID-switch check's dialog), `ui/loading-progress-modal.js` (the
per-video loading bars), `ui/origin-rebase.js` (Set as New Calibration),
`ui/export-modals.js` (Choose Frames for Triangulation, Export 3D Video) and
`ui/overlay-export-modal.js` (the overlay video export). NOT position bars: the
video seekbar, an ID-switch row's playhead, range sliders — they show where,
not how far along. A new progress bar calls `noteProgress` after each update. The loading
overlay's bar and the image check dialog's bar are a deep blue (`#1f78b4`) so
the lit stretch stands out against them.

**Imports from project modules.** None. **Imported by.** The callers above.

Tested by `tests/e2e/progress-delta.mjs` (the tint's extent on both kinds of
fill, its brightness from screenshot pixels, its edge following the fill's
frame by frame mid-transition, and no tint on a move back, a snap or a hide).

### ui/loading-overlay.js

**Purpose.** Owns the full-window loading overlay (`#loadingOverlay`) and its
determinate progress bar — the ONE progress component shared by Track All,
Triangulate All (all three routes), Group by Identity/Track, File ▸ Save, and anything else
that runs a chunked main-thread loop behind the overlay.

**It is a card in the image ID-switch check's dialog style**
(`ui/id-switch-modal.js`): an opaque `.loading-card` with an optional title
(`#loadingTitle`), the message, the ETA, the bar with its playhead
(`#loadingProgressHead`), the percentage under it (`#loadingPct`; shared rule
with `.id-switch-progress-pct`), then the detail line and the CPU row, all
left-aligned. The full-window `.loading-overlay` under it is TRANSPARENT but
still blocks clicks while an operation runs (Triangulate All resolves its
settings once and relies on that). The spinner shows only for a message with
no bar. Pinned by `tests/e2e/fixtures/progress-card.js`.

**Key exports.**
- `showLoading(msg, { title, step, steps, detail })` — indeterminate form
  (spinner + text); hides the bar and the percentage. `step`/`steps`/`detail`
  fill the detail line as in `showLoadingProgress`, for a step of a multi-step
  run that cannot count its own work (Save's file-writing steps, Open project's
  spinner steps); without them the detail line is hidden, as before.
- **The title** (`opts.title` on either call) names the operation — "Track All",
  "Triangulate All", "Checking ID switches (body size)" — and is KEPT until
  `hideLoading` (or a call passing another; `''` clears it), so the progress
  calls deep inside a run, and a message mid-run, need not repeat it; each
  operation passes it on its first call only. An operation that passes none
  shows none.
- `showLoadingProgress(label, done, total, { step, steps, detail, unit, title })` —
  writes the standard `"<label>: done/total frames…"` line (counts via
  `toLocaleString('en-US')`), the floored percentage under the bar (100% means
  done), moves fill and playhead by `transform`, and drives the bar
  (`#loadingProgress` / `#loadingProgressFill`, `role="progressbar"`).
  `#loadingSubstatus` carries `Step k of n` (only when `steps > 1`) and
  `detail`, so the main line keeps one format everywhere. A new stage (bar
  hidden, or the fraction moving backwards) snaps instead of animating.
- **Time remaining** (`#loadingEta`) — "Estimating time remaining…" until the
  stage has run 1.5 s and covered 1%, then "About 1 min 10 s remaining" ("… in
  this step" when `steps > 1`), cleared at 100%. The rate is measured over the
  last 10 s of the CURRENT stage (a new label/step/total, or backwards progress,
  restarts it). `estimateRemainingMs(samples, done, total)`,
  `formatRemaining(ms)` and the clock hook `__setLoadingOverlayClock(fn)` are
  exported for tests; the raw estimate is `#loadingEta[data-ms]`.
- `hideLoading()` — hides the overlay and resets the bar (and stops `setLoadingCpu`).
- `setLoadingCpu(source)` — a CPU line and last-minute graph under the detail
  line (`#loadingHardware` = `#loadingCpu` + `#loadingGraph`), sampled every
  `CPU_SAMPLE_MS` as busy thread-ms / cores via `createLoadMeter`; the tooltip
  names `source.counts`. A source has `cpuBusyMs()` and/or `mainThread: true`,
  for which the overlay clocks the main thread itself (`createBusyClock`): busy
  EXCEPT while waiting in `yieldToPaint` or `idleWhile` (overlapping waits are
  counted, so the lane reopens once). Both given, the line is their SUM — Track
  All on the tracker worker passes `{mainThread: true, cpuBusyMs:
  pool.cpuBusyMs}`; inline, `{mainThread: true}` alone. CPU only. Uses the same
  `ui/load-graph.js` parts as the image check's dialog; the graph is built once
  and `clear()`ed per operation. In Brave with fingerprinting protection,
  `#loadingBraveHint` (`renderBraveFingerprintingHint`) explains the random core
  count. Runs until `hideLoading()` / `setLoadingCpu(null)`; a throwing source
  stops it. The body-size check is left out ON PURPOSE: it is one
  uninterrupted main-thread block, so a 1 s sampler could never read a load. A
  CPU line is worth adding only to an operation that runs for seconds AND yields.
- `idleWhile(promise)` — awaits `promise` as main-thread IDLE time for a
  `{mainThread}` CPU line (the main thread waiting on a worker is not using the
  CPU), sharing `yieldToPaint`'s wait count. No line: just the promise.
- `busyDuring(fn)` — runs `fn` counted as main-thread CPU time even inside an
  `idleWhile` / `yieldToPaint` wait: the tracker worker's results are committed
  from its message handler, which runs during exactly those waits.
- `createProgressPacer(intervalMs = PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MS)` → `{ due(), yield() }`.
  `due()` is true at most once per interval (a `performance.now()` check, cheap
  enough to call every iteration); `yield()` = `yieldToPaint()`, after which the
  interval restarts (so it measures WORK time between yields).
- `yieldToPaint()` — (also the wait a `{mainThread}` CPU line leaves out)
  resolves after the browser has painted: rAF then a task queued after it (a
  bare `setTimeout(0)` can fire before the next frame), with a 100 ms timer
  fallback. A hidden tab yields through a `MessageChannel` (not throttled to 1/s
  like background timers).
- `PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MS` (250), `CPU_SAMPLE_MS` (1000).

**Imports from project modules.** `./progress-delta.js` (`noteProgress` — the
bar's lit stretch, called from `_setFill`; import-free), `./load-graph.js` (`createLoadMeter`,
`createBusyClock`, `createLoadGraph`, `formatCpuLoad`, `setLoadLine`, `cpuCores` —
itself import-free and DOM-free at load), `./browser-hints.js`
(`renderBraveFingerprintingHint`; also import-free and DOM-free at load). Safe under the Node test stubs: every DOM access is
null-guarded (no `#loadingHardware` → `setLoadingCpu` does nothing), and with no
`requestAnimationFrame` it yields via `MessageChannel`.

**Imported by.** `import-export/save-load.js` (re-exports `showLoading`/
`hideLoading`), `pose/tracker.js`, `pose/triangulation.js`, `ui/export-modals.js`,
`ui/id-switch-modal.js`, `loading/session-loader.js` (Load Single Session
Folder's three-step progress).

**Notes / caveats.**
- **The fill animates `transform: scaleX()`** under a 250 ms linear transition
  (matched to `PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MS`), which runs on the compositor and keeps
  gliding while the main thread works. Animating `width` would freeze between
  updates.
- Cost ≈ one display frame per 250 ms; `tests/e2e/_bench-progress-overlay.mjs`
  measures it.
- Not to be confused with `ui/loading-progress-modal.js`, the per-camera,
  multi-row panel used for video loading.

---

### ui/load-graph.js

**Purpose.** How busy a long operation keeps the CPU (and the GPU), for its
progress UI: the busy clock its threads report to, the rolling load meter, the
"CPU: 12 cores · load 7%" line, and a small graph of the last minute. Shared by
the image check's progress dialog (`ui/id-switch-modal.js`: CPU and GPU lines,
both series) and the loading overlay (`ui/loading-overlay.js` `setLoadingCpu`:
the CPU alone — Triangulate All and Track All). No browser API reports the CPU's or a GPU's
utilisation, so every load is the operation's OWN busy time as a share of the
hardware, and each line's tooltip says other apps are not in it.

**Key exports.**
- `LOAD_WINDOW_MS` (5000); `createLoadMeter(windowMs)` -> `(now, busyMs) ->
  pct|null` (moved here from `ui/image-embedder.js`, which re-exports it) — the
  share of the window spent busy; for the CPU, feed thread-ms / cores.
- `createBusyClock(now?)` -> `{begin(lane), end(lane), close(lane?), ms()}` —
  busy time summed over lanes (threads); nested per lane (a crop worker holds
  several queued jobs; a pool's `'main'` is reopened by a reply handled while it
  waits); open spans count up to now; `close()` with no argument closes every lane.
- `formatCpuLoad(cores, load, counts)` -> `{text, title, load}` — "CPU: 12 cores ·
  load 7%" (`cores` = `navigator.hardwareConcurrency`, logical processors; one
  saturated thread on 12 cores reads 8%); the tooltip is "The share of the CPU's
  12 cores kept busy over the last 5 s by " + `counts` + ". Browsers do not report
  the CPU's total load, so other apps are not included." `cpuCores()`.
- `LOAD_GRAPH_SPAN_MS` (60 s); `loadGraphPath(samples, now, spanMs, w, h)` -> SVG
  path data, placed by TIME (ticks are uneven: long work blocks the 1 s timer),
  broken at nulls, a lone point as a zero-length segment (a dot under the round cap).
- `LOAD_GRAPH_W` / `LOAD_GRAPH_H` / `LOAD_GRAPH_PAD` (84 x 34 px plot, series
  inset 2 px so 0%/100% are not half clipped). 84 px keeps the image check's
  "GPU: WebGPU, fp16 · load 100%" on one line in its 540 px dialog.
  `LOAD_GRAPH_TICK` (4 px): the axis ticks' length.
- `createLoadGraph(box)` -> `{show(on), push(t, cpu, gpu), clear()}` — builds the
  graph into `box` (class `load-graph`): a 50% hairline, CPU and GPU series, and
  **labelled axes** ("100%/50%/0%", "60 s ago … now") **with ticks pointing OUT
  of the plot**. Ticks are absolutely positioned spans outside the SVG, because
  the SVG must keep `overflow: hidden` (points older than the span sit left of
  it). The y labels are ZERO-HEIGHT boxes spread over a column sized from the
  constants, so each centres on its level. Axes are `aria-hidden`; the SVG's
  `aria-label` says the same. On hover: a cursor, a dot per series and a
  readout for the nearest MEASURED sample, kept current under a still pointer.
  `show(false)` is `visibility`, so the space stays reserved.
- `setLoadLine(el, text, title, series)` — a line's text and tooltip, and its
  legend key (`.load-line.has-series::before`, in `.is-cpu` / `.is-gpu` colour).

**Styles.** `styles.css` `.load-line` / `.load-graph*`; the series colours are
`:root` `--load-cpu` `#3987e5` and `--load-gpu` `#d95926`, the first two slots
of the dataviz skill's validated categorical palette (dark steps), checked
against the plot's `#1a1a1a` (CVD dE 26.8).

**Imports from project modules.** None, and no DOM at load time:
`ui/image-embedder.js` imports it and is also loaded by the crop and model workers.

**Imported by.** `ui/image-embedder.js`, `ui/id-switch-modal.js`,
`ui/loading-overlay.js`, `pose/triangulation-pool.js`, `pose/tracker-pool.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-load-graph.mjs`;
`tests/e2e/fixtures/load-graph-axes.js` (labels and ticks, in all three places
the graph appears); `tests/e2e/id-switch-image-check.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/triangulate-all-cpu-load.mjs` and `tests/e2e/track-all-cpu-load.mjs`.

---


### ui/layout-controls.js

**Purpose.** Resizable split-handle bar between video grid, 3D viewport,
info panel, and timeline.

**Key exports.**
- `setupDragHandle(handle, onDrag)` — attaches mouse-drag listener to a
  split-handle DOM element.
- `setupSplitHandles()` — wires every split handle in the page.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./app-state.js` — `viewport3d`, `timeline`.
- `./ui-wiring.js` — `syncTimelineToggleButton`,
  `updateInfoPanelToggleBtn`, `toggleInfoPanel`,
  `update3DViewportToggleBtn`, `toggle3DViewport`.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`.

**User-facing features.** Drag-to-resize panel boundaries between video
grid / 3D / info-panel / timeline. Also wires the two toolbar panel-toggle
buttons (`#infoPanelToggleBtn`, `#viewport3dToggleBtn`) and keeps their state
in sync from the `MutationObserver` that watches the 3D container's and info
wrapper's `class` attributes — so a collapse from any entry point (button, `\`,
View menu) updates both buttons, and the initial state is correct.

---

### ui/modal-geometry.js

**Purpose.** Remembered size and position for resizable modals.

Gives a modal card a drag handle, a resize grip (CSS `resize: both`) and a
`localStorage` record of where it ended up.

**Key exports.**
- `clampGeometry(geom, viewport, opts)` — pure, and load-bearing: a remembered
  rect may be restored into a much smaller viewport, and applied blindly it
  puts the header (drag handle AND close button) off-screen. Size clamps to
  `[min, available]` (available wins), position keeps the whole card inside,
  `opts.margin` keeps a gutter. Returns `null` for anything unusable, so the
  caller centres instead.
- `centerGeometry(w, h, viewport, opts)` — first-open placement.
- `readGeometry(id, storage)` / `writeGeometry(id, geom, storage)` /
  `clearGeometry(id, storage)` — the `lucid.modalGeometry.v1` record, keyed by
  modal id so one modal cannot clobber another. Reads are **not** clamped:
  clamping on the way in would let one small window permanently shrink the
  remembered size.
- `installModalGeometry(card, opts)` — restores, wires the header drag, watches
  the CSS resize via `ResizeObserver`, re-clamps on window resize, and returns a
  `dispose()` that also flushes the final rect (a resize that ended inside the
  200 ms persist debounce would otherwise be lost when the modal closes).
- `rememberedSizeIsChosen(geom, legacyDefaultSize)` — whether a record's size
  was the user's choice (see below).

**Only a size the user chose is remembered.** Position always is; size only
once the user resized the card with its grip (the `ResizeObserver` sees a size
other than the one `apply()` set, and the record gets `sized: true`). Otherwise
the card opens at the stylesheet's size, so changing the default reaches
everyone who never resized. Records from before the flag saved the opening size
on every close: `opts.legacyDefaultSize` names that old default, and such a
record opens at the new default, centred where the old card was. Settings passes
`{w: 880, h: 620}` (its default before it widened to 1040px).

**This module is the ONE owner of the card's rect.** `.settings-modal`
deliberately has no `max-width`/`max-height`, and `apply()` sets both to `none`
inline: a stylesheet cap alongside the clamp would silently shrink a width the
clamp thought it had granted, landing the card flush against one edge with a gap
on the opposite side.

**Geometry is browser-local display taste**: `localStorage`, NOT the `.slp`.
Blocked or full storage degrades to "forgets", never to a throw.

**Imports from project modules.** None — `clampGeometry` is a pure function of
(rect, viewport), so it bridges into both test runners.

**Imported by.** `ui/settings-modal.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-modal-geometry.js` and
`tests/e2e/settings-modal-geometry.mjs`.

---

### ui/overlay-export-layout.js

**Purpose.** The pure half of "Export Video Overlays" (issue #190):
composition geometry, output sizing, encoder parameters, the persisted settings
schema, and the seed-layout plan. Deliberately **dependency-free** — no project
imports, no dockview, no DOM beyond `localStorage` — so it can be bridged into
the classic-script unit runner and exercised without a browser dock.

**Key exports.**
- `TILE_3D` — the synthetic view name (`'__3d__'`) of the 3D viewport tile.
- `fitRect(srcW, srcH, dstW, dstH)` — aspect-preserving "contain" fit. **Must
  stay numerically identical to `videoToCanvas()` in `ui/overlays.js`**: the
  video is drawn with this and the skeleton with that, so any divergence offsets
  every burned-in overlay from the animal. Pinned by
  `tests/test-overlay-export-layout.js`, which computes both and compares.
- `distributeAxisSizes(base, min, max, vis, k, d, mode)` + `SASH_SHARE_FAR` /
  `SASH_GROW_ONE` / `SASH_SHARE_SIDES` — sizes for one dock AXIS after dragging the
  sash at index `k` by `d` px, so a resize is shared by more than the one adjacent
  tile (dockview 6.6.1 hands the whole delta to the neighbour, unconfigurably).
  **Governing constraint:** a sash sits at the running SUM of the sizes before it,
  so {sash tracks cursor, dragged tile 1:1, EVERY other tile shares} is
  **geometrically impossible** for any sash but the first — hence three modes.
  **`'share-far'` is what the modal uses**: tile `k` changes by exactly the drag
  and only the tiles BEYOND the sash share the opposite change; tiles before it
  never move, so the handle tracks the cursor and both grow-one and shrink-one
  stay expressible. **Accepted gap:** the LAST sash has one tile beyond it, so
  it is neighbour-only — pinned by a unit test as a decision.
  `'grow-one'` (every tile shares) is NOT used: the sash trails the cursor by an
  amount depending on which sash you grabbed. `'share-sides'` tracks the cursor
  but has no shrink-one gesture.
  The result **always sums to `sum(base)`** (unit-tested over `k`, `d` and all
  modes) — a drifting total would move the exported frame.
  `fillEvenly` shares **with spill**: a tile at its 100px minimum holds still and
  the others absorb its share. It deliberately does NOT veto the drag (a seeded
  dock routinely has a tile at the minimum; a veto makes the sash feel dead).
- `rotatedBoxSize(srcW, srcH, rotationDeg)` / `rotatedFit(srcW, srcH, dstW, dstH,
  rotationDeg)` — the rotation-aware fit: a 90° camera rotation SWAPS a view's
  effective aspect (the main window rotates the whole `.canvas-wrapper`).
  Cardinal angles are special-cased so 90/270 swap **exactly** (trig dust would
  make an output dimension odd, which H.264 rejects). `rotatedFit` returns the
  scale plus the UNROTATED box (`boxWidth`/`boxHeight`) that a tile draw paints
  into and then rotates. **At rotation 0 it equals `fitRect` exactly** — pinned
  by a test; that identity is the no-regression guarantee for unrotated projects.
- `computeTileRects(dock, tiles, outW, outH)` — maps dock-local tile rects into
  the output canvas (the dock is fitted first, so a mismatched output aspect
  letterboxes the whole composition rather than distorting it). Integer pixels,
  clamped into bounds, never degenerate.
- `outputSizeFor(aspect, presetKey)` — height from `RES_PRESETS`, width from the
  aspect, clamped to `MAX_OUT_DIM` (3840) with the height recomputed so the aspect
  holds. **Always even**: `VideoEncoder` rejects odd H.264 (yuv420) dimensions, so
  an odd size is a hard export failure.
  `RES_PRESETS` is the **one tier table both video-export modals use** —
  **480 / 720 / 1080 / 2160**, keys ARE the height (`ui/export-modals.js` builds
  `V3D_RES` from it). Each `refW` is the 16:9 width: exact for the always-16:9
  3D viewport, a label hint here. `2160`'s `refW` (3840) is exactly
  `MAX_OUT_DIM`; any smaller cap and 16:9 4K would silently clamp (pinned by a
  test). `sanitizeSettings` maps a retired key to `DEFAULT_RES`, not the
  nearest tier, so a reopen cannot silently multiply the next export's size.
- `outputSizeFrom(settings, aspect)` / `RES_CUSTOM` / `clampOutDim(n)` /
  `customAspect(settings)` — the editable-dimensions path. `res: 'custom'` uses
  `settings.outW` × `settings.outH` verbatim instead of deriving the width from
  the composition; `clampOutDim` puts a hand-typed value through the same
  even/2..`MAX_OUT_DIM` gate the presets satisfy by construction.
  `customAspect` returns the aspect the modal must **shape the dock to** (null
  for a preset, where dock and output agree already) — shaping it is what stops
  `computeTileRects` from burning letterbox bars into a custom-size export.
- `bitrateIsClamped(W, H, fps, quality)` — true when `bitrateFor`'s [1, 48] Mbps
  band bit, making adjacent Quality tiers **collide** (e.g. 4K60 `medium` =
  `high`). The modal's output note surfaces it, or the picker looks broken.
- `h264CodecFor(W, H)`, `bitrateFor(W, H, fps, quality)`, `QUALITY_BPP`. **Both
  video-export modals call these** — `showExport3DVideoModal`'s `_v3dBitrate` is
  just `bitrateFor(W, H, fps, 'medium')`; keep it that way rather than a private
  copy with its own clamp band.
- `estimatedBytes(W, H, fps, quality, nFrames)` — expected size of one output
  file. Both modals' summary lines AND both streaming decisions read this, so
  the size the UI promises cannot drift from the size the export plans for.
  Returns 0 for a degenerate fps/range rather than `NaN`/`Infinity`.
- `shouldStreamToDisk(totalBytes)` / `STREAM_TO_DISK_BYTES` (256 MB) — the
  buffer-vs-stream split. Under it an export buffers and downloads (no
  destination prompt); over it, it streams into a picked file/folder, since that
  much output buffered in the pointer-compressed heap risks a renderer OOM.
  Used by BOTH video modals.
- `defaultOverlayExportSettings()`, `mergeSettings(base, saved)`,
  `sanitizeSettings(s)`, `applyStoredSettings`, `saveOverlayExportSettings`,
  `SETTINGS_KEY` (`'overlayExportSettings.v1'`), `DEFAULT_RES` (`'1080'`).
  `mergeSettings` is schema-driven: it ignores undeclared keys and values whose
  `typeof` doesn't match, so a stale blob cannot slip a string into an encoder
  parameter. It only type-checks, so `applyStoredSettings` follows it with
  `sanitizeSettings`, which holds `res` to the *current* preset set (an unknown
  key would blank the `<select>` while the summary quoted a size it doesn't
  name), and **folds a stored `labelSize` of 0 into `showLabels: false` with the
  default size** (issue #223: old blobs encoded "off" as 0; unreachable from the
  current modal).
  `UNRESTORED_KEYS` (`res`, `outW`, `outH`) are **written but never read back**,
  so the modal always opens at `DEFAULT_RES` = **1080p**: the tier decides file
  size, so a departure from 1080 must be a choice made in front of the current
  summary. `outW`/`outH` go with it so "Custom" cannot resurrect an old size.
  Everything else (layers, styling, fps, quality, mode) IS restored.
  `tests/e2e/overlay-export-modal.mjs` asserts both the write and the reset.
- `overlayOptionsFrom(settings, videoW, videoH, canvasW, canvasH)` — the
  settings → `drawFrameOverlays()` options translation (`trailShade` passes
  through as its key; `drawNodeTrails` resolves it). The default
  `trailShade: 'dark'` repeats `DEFAULT_TRAIL_SHADE` because this module imports
  nothing; `tests/test-overlay-export-layout.js` holds the two together. Explicitly nulls ALL
  interaction state (selection / hover / drag / assignment): an export has no
  cursor, and a stray highlight would be burned into the video.
  **Node labels are gated by `showLabels`, never by their size** (issue #223):
  `settings.user.showLabels` / `settings.reproj.showLabels` are real booleans;
  `labelSize` is only a size. Defaults are user **on** / reproj **off**,
  matching the Visibility panel. **Predicted has no toggle because the app has
  no such layer** (`ui/rendering.js` hardcodes `predictedOpts.showLabels:
  false`). `showLabels` also gates the TRACK-name labels (`drawInstanceLabels`),
  as in the live app.
- `seedLayoutPlan(viewNames, include3D)` — the mirror-the-main-window seed (same
  row-count heuristic as `addAllViewsAsGrid`, 3D docked right of the whole grid).
  Returns add-panel steps whose positions reference **earlier** entries by index,
  so the caller can substitute real dockview panel ids as it walks forward.

**Imports from project modules.** None (by design).

**Imported by.** `ui/overlay-export-modal.js`; `ui/export-modals.js`
(`shouldStreamToDisk`, so both video exports share one threshold); bridged into
`tests/test-runner.html` as `window.__OverlayExportLayout`.

---

### ui/overlay-export-modal.js

**Purpose.** The "Export Video Overlays" modal (File menu, above "Export 3D
Video") — issue #190. Renders the session's 2D camera videos **with pose
overlays burned in**, plus the 3D viewport, either stitched into one composed
video laid out exactly as the user arranged it, or as one file per tile. The
counterpart of SLEAP's `View ▸ Render Video Clip with Instances…`
(`sleap/gui/dialogs/render_clip.py`), extended with LUCID's multi-view
composition and its user / predicted / reprojected overlay layers.

**Key exports.** `showOverlayExportModal()`, `settingsFromVisibilityPanel()`,
`loadOverlayExportSettings()`, and a re-export of `TILE_3D`.
The node-trail shade is seeded from `state.trailShade` and offered as the Layers
group's **Trail shade** select (`#ovTrailShade`, the five `TRAIL_SHADES`), right
under Node trails; like the trail length it is then remembered with the export's
own settings. `loadOverlayExportSettings` replaces a stored shade this build does
not offer with `DEFAULT_TRAIL_SHADE`, which would otherwise leave the select blank.

**Layout.** `[views strip] [composition dock] [settings panel]`. The strip lists
the **3D grid FIRST**, then one thumbnail per video; drag or double-click to dock.
A docked entry is **highlighted** (accent border, matching
`.session-strip-item.active`; fading would read as *disabled*) by
`refreshStripHighlights()`, run from exactly the three sites that mutate the
`tiles` map behind `isDocked`: `buildStrip()`, `TilePane.init` (dockview calls it
from every `addPanel`, even an inactive stacked tab) and `onDidRemovePanel`. A
panel MOVE needs no hook: it keeps its id and fires neither event.
**Strip order is independent of DOCK order**: the 3D tile is still SEEDED last, a
column right of the video grid (`seedLayoutPlan`) — reordering the strip must
not shift existing users' exported layouts. Pinned by
`tests/e2e/overlay-export-modal.mjs` and `tests/test-overlay-export-layout.js`'s
"3D docked last".
**`qualityPhrase(W, H, fps, approx)`** builds the Quality half of the output note
(tier, real Mbps, "capped" from `bitrateIsClamped`) in BOTH modes — bitrate is
the one setting a still preview cannot show, so this text is the picker's only
feedback.
The middle is its **own `DockviewComponent` instance** (`dockview-core@6.6.1` —
**three** pins share that version: `index.html`'s CSS, `sessions-panes.js`, and
this module), seeded via `seedLayoutPlan` to mirror the main window. The
settings panel: frame range (**1-based display**, 0-based internally), layers,
per-layer appearance, background, quality/output.
**`Show node labels`** (issue #223) sits in the User and Reprojection Appearance
groups; `Node label size` floors at **1** (the toggle is the off switch).
`settingsFromVisibilityPanel` SPLITS the panel's folded encoding: a panel size
of 0 seeds `showLabels: false` with the default size — copying the 0 would
leave the toggle able to turn on nothing. There is no Predicted entry; see
`overlayOptionsFrom` above.

**Output dimensions.** A Resolution picker (the four `RES_PRESETS` tiers plus
**Custom**) and a live `width × height` row; Quality is a *separate* bitrate
tier (`QUALITY_BPP`). `resTierLabel()` names the tier next to the literal `W×H`
because they can disagree: a preset fixes only the HEIGHT, so a very wide
composition is width-capped (`2160p (width-capped)`). The fields always show the
real output size (`syncOutFields()` from `refreshSummary()`), and typing in one
switches to Custom from those numbers. A custom size re-shapes the **dock** to
that aspect (`applyDockAspect`, also on a `ResizeObserver` on `#ovDockFrame`) so
`computeTileRects` fills rather than letterboxes. In individual mode a custom
size applies to every file, each tile letterboxed into it.

**Reprojection fill.** `ensureReprojections(f, groups, session)` fills frame
`f`'s reprojections and errors so a never-viewed range still draws them. It
**is** `ui/rendering.js`'s `fillLazyReprojections`, not a copy: a private
re-solve would draw different numbers from the app's stored 3D and keep derived
state for EVERY exported frame; sharing gives "exported 3D == displayed 3D" and
the `DERIVED_FRAMES_KEEP` cap. A no-op for a session other than `state.session`.
Covered by `tests/e2e/load-export-move-bounded-derived-state.mjs` (E).

**Still-frame previewer, no playback.** **No transport** (no play/pause,
stepping, `setPlaying`/`playTimer`): playback would decode every docked view per
tick and compete with the export. Scrub the track, or use the range fields for a
precise frame.

**Preview ↔ app viewer.** Scrubbing drives the real viewer through
`videoController.scrubToFrame()` (`syncViewer`, from `showFrame`), the
**coalescing** entry point, so a fast drag queues no decode backlog and closing
leaves you on that frame. Suppressed while exporting.

**Track gestures** are separated by *what you press* (`dragging`:
`'playhead' | 'start' | 'end'`). Pressing the track scrubs the **current
frame**; an endpoint moves only when its handle (above the track via `z-index`,
claiming its own `pointerdown`) is grabbed — a track click must never redefine
what will be exported. Scrubbing is deliberately **not** clamped to the range.
Releasing an endpoint previews the boundary it was dropped on.

**Rendering model.** Every tile owns **two** canvases (video + overlay), like
the main window, because `drawFrameOverlays()` opens with a full-canvas
`clearRect`. Export allocates **separate** canvases at the output pixel box, so
it never disturbs the preview. Geometry is read off the DOM (`captureLayout` →
`computeTileRects`), making the stitched output WYSIWYG. A tile <2px (hidden
stacked tab) is skipped. `addTile` with no position splits right rather than
stacking, since a stacked tab would be invisible.

**3D tile.** A second `Viewport3D` (`preserveDrawingBuffer: true`), as in
`showExport3DVideoModal`. For export its renderer is `setSize(w, h, false)`'d to
the output tile box (CSS size untouched) with the container `ResizeObserver`
**disconnected**, then restored afterwards.

**Encoding.** Delegated entirely to **`ui/video-encode.js`** (mediabunny), shared
with the 3D video export — this module never touches `VideoEncoder` or a
muxer. Individual mode runs N writers inside the SAME frame loop (each frame
decoded once); `await writer.addFrame()` applies backpressure.
**Destination:** above `shouldStreamToDisk` it asks for a save-file (stitched)
or a folder (individual; the folder handle is deliberately NOT cached to
`state.exportDirHandle`, which `showSlpExportAllModal` reuses without
prompting) and streams; below, it buffers and downloads. Declining the picker
for a large export **cancels** it rather than falling back to the memory buffer;
with no File System Access API the user gets `exportLabels()`'s `confirm()`
warning. Cancelling a streamed export leaves a partial `.mp4` on disk, which the
status message says.

**Settings persistence.** Seeded from the Visibility panel
(`getVisibilitySettings()`) on first open, then persisted to `localStorage`. The
**layout** is deliberately NOT persisted; a reopen re-seeds from the main window.

**Sash drags are taken over from dockview.** A capture-phase `pointerdown` on
`#ovDock` re-implements sash drags via `distributeAxisSizes` and
`stopPropagation()`s, so dockview's own sash listener never runs (one handler,
nothing to fight). dockview 6.6.1 offers no option for this, and post-correcting
in `onDidLayoutChange` would jump on release (it fires once at drag end). An
axis of two tiles with nothing to keep aligned is left to dockview (identical
arithmetic).

**A row divider moves that boundary in EVERY column** (`axesForSash`). dockview
nests a 4+ camera grid **column-major**, so a native row sash resizes only its
own column and the grid drifts ragged. `axesForSash` returns the dragged axis
**plus every sibling axis with a boundary at the same index `k`**, all moved by
the same delta; a sibling too short to have boundary `k` (full-height camera,
3D tile) is left alone. The ROOT axis has no siblings, so a column drag only
resizes columns and a row drag never changes a width.
This reaches **TypeScript-private dockview internals** — `gridview.root`, node
`children` / `element` (walked to find the branch owning the sash **and its
parent**), `BranchNode.splitview`, `Splitview.viewItems` / `sashes` /
`layoutViews()` / `distributeEmptySpace()` / `saveProportions()`, and
`viewItem.enabled`. Every one is feature-detected: if any is missing the handler
returns WITHOUT `stopPropagation()` and dockview's stock drag runs, so a bump
can regress the behaviour but cannot break resizing.
**Run both e2e files:** `tests/e2e/overlay-export-sash-distribute.mjs` (sharing
on a flat axis; two drags, so an "equalise everything" fix fails) only drags
sash 0, so it is blind to cursor tracking and the nested grid, which
`tests/e2e/overlay-export-sash-tracking.mjs` covers (fails on `grow-one`).

**The tab band is overlaid on the tile, not stacked above it** (`styles.css`,
`#ovDock`): the band is shortened to 20px and `.dv-content-container` pulled up
by exactly that, so the tile gets the group's full height instead of losing a
band per grid row. The real tab is KEPT: `.dv-tab` is dockview's **drag source**,
and `captureLayout()` reads the arrangement it produces. `position: relative` +
`z-index: 2` on the band is load-bearing (the absolutely-positioned canvases
would otherwise swallow tab clicks). Scoped to `#ovDock` (the abyss theme is
shared with the main dock) and declared on `.dv-groupview` (dockview's own
`.dv-shell` re-declares the theme inside the container).

**The tab shows the ✕, never the name** — a visible title would double the
burned-in `Render Video Names` caption. `.dv-default-tab-content` is `width: 0`;
with the layer OFF the name appears on **hover**. `order: 2` puts the title
AFTER the ✕ so the reveal grows rightward and the ✕ never moves (the e2e pins
its x in all four layer × hover states). A 12px `padding-left` leaves bare
`.dv-tab` (the drag source) left of the ✕; `min-width: 44px` is a floor against
a dockview padding change.
`applyTabNameMode()` toggles `#ovDock.ov-hover-tab-names` from `onChange` AND
`buildSettings` (Reset calls only the latter).
The hover is keyed on **`.dv-groupview:hover`, NOT the tile**: the tab band is a
*sibling* of `.dv-content-container`, so a tile `:hover` would drop as the
pointer reaches the ✕. With the layer ON the band is `translateY`'d one
band-height so the ✕ sits below the caption (`transform`, so layout is
