unaffected). The 3D tile keeps its name always (never captioned), via a
separate `:has()` rule so an engine without `:has()` keeps the hover reveal.

**Display settings are inherited from the main window, not re-exposed.** Every
2D tile is drawn with that camera's **brightness, contrast, rotation and zoom**
by one shared `drawTileContent(vctx, octx, …)` that both the preview
(`paintTile`) and the export (`drawExportTile`) call. The modal has **no**
control for any of them (`settings.reproj.brightness` is an unrelated marker
dimming), and says so in `#ovDisplayNote`.
The main window applies all four as **CSS on live elements**, which does not
survive a `drawImage`, so the tile re-applies them: brightness/contrast via
**`ctx.filter`** with `buildVideoFilter`'s exact string; rotation and zoom via a
context transform on **video and overlays together** (transforming only the
pixels would slide every skeleton off the animal). Load-bearing details:
- clear the overlay canvas under the **identity** transform first —
  `drawFrameOverlays`'s `clearRect` clears a *rotated* rect otherwise, leaving
  stale skeleton in the corners;
- draw the legend **outside** the transform — `drawLegend` anchors to
  `ctx.canvas.width`, so inside it drifts by the letterbox offset and flips at 180°;
- reset `ctx.filter` with `'none'`, never `''` (silently ignored, leaking the
  video's brightness onto the overlay). `buildVideoFilter` returns `''` at defaults.
Rotation prefers `view.rotation` over the session value (mid hold-to-rotate the
view is ahead of the commit); a never-docked view falls back to the session.
`individualSizeFor` uses the **rotated** box, so a 90°-rotated view exports
portrait.
**Zoom fidelity is partial, by construction:** magnification and pan centre
match; the **crop cannot** (an export tile has its own aspect, so it shows more
video, possibly black past a frame edge). The transform mirrors `applyZoom`'s
CSS under `transform-origin: 0 0` — hence the `(z-1)*box/2` term and the
`box/baseW` pan conversion. Covered by
`tests/e2e/overlay-export-display-settings.mjs`, a separate file because the
main modal e2e is decoder-less and black pixels cannot detect any of this.

**Unlinked instances ARE drawn** by `collectUnlinked(frameGroup, viewName)`,
from the RAW `FrameGroup` (`getUnlinkedInstances()`, not the
`toOverlayFrameGroup` copy), filtered by the User/Predictions layers. It is
assigned as `opts.unlinkedInstances = …`, not in the `overlayOptionsFrom`
literal — a grep for `unlinkedInstances:` misses it.
`overlayOptionsFrom` pins **`showUnlinkedBadge: false`**, so the skeleton is
exported but the amber `?` editing prompt is not.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./progress-delta.js` — `noteProgress` (the lit stretch on the export's bar).
- `./app-state.js` — `state`, `videoController`, `getActiveSession`.
- `./browser-hints.js` — `fileSystemAccessHint` (appended to the in-memory
  export confirm in Brave).
- `./viewport3d.js` — `Viewport3D`.
- `./overlays.js` — `drawFrameOverlays`, `drawLegend`, `drawViewNameLabel`,
  `getTrackColor`, `getGroupColor`, `TRAIL_SHADES`, `DEFAULT_TRAIL_SHADE`,
  `trailShadeFor`.
- `./overlay-export-layout.js` — the pure helpers above.
- `./video-encode.js` — `createMp4Writer`, `videoEncodingAvailable`.
- `./video-filters.js` — `buildVideoFilter`, `getSessionBrightness`,
  `getSessionContrast`, `getSessionRotation`. That module imports no project
  modules, so this adds no cycle, and sharing `buildVideoFilter` with
  `applyVideoFilters` is what keeps the export from drifting from the live view.
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`,
  `ensureLazyFrameData`, `ensureLazyTrailWindow` (the frames behind the first
  exported frame and the preview frame that the node trails draw),
  `sessionHasCalibration`.
- `./rendering.js` — `getVisibilitySettings` (the seed), `fillLazyReprojections`
  (the reprojection fill, see above).
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `setStatus`.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`menuExportOverlayVideo`).

**Tests.** `tests/test-overlay-export-layout.js` and
`tests/e2e/overlay-export-modal.mjs` (incl. a **real** mp4 export, stitched /
individual / custom-size, with the `avc1` dimensions asserted against
`outputSizeFor`). The track-gesture split is driven with real mouse events
(it is about hit-testing); the discriminating assertion is that a track press
leaves the export range *unchanged*.

### ui/video-encode.js

**Purpose.** The app's **only** video-encoding seam. Both video exports
("Export Video Overlays", "Export 3D Video") go through it; neither constructs a
`VideoEncoder` or a muxer.

**Why it is not sleap-io.js.** sleap-io.js has **no browser video encoder**
(`renderVideo()` shells out to native `ffmpeg`, Node entry only). So LUCID owns
encoding, thinly, on **mediabunny** — the library sleap-io.js uses for decode,
already vendored (`lib/mediabunny/`, importmap `mediabunny`). Do not
reintroduce a second muxer.

**Key exports.**
- `createMp4Writer({canvas, width, height, fps, bitrate, frameCount,
  fullCodecString, keyFrameEveryFrames, fileHandle})` → `{addFrame(outIdx),
  finish(), cancel(), streaming, codec, fastStart, framesAdded}`. The writer is
  bound to ONE canvas, sampled at each `addFrame`.
- `resolveH264Config(width, height, bitrate, preferredCodecString)` — probes with
  `canEncodeVideo` and degrades to mediabunny's own level pick if the caller's
  exact H.264 level string isn't encodable here. Returns `null` when H.264 is
  unavailable at that size, which is what turns into the user-facing error.
- `videoEncodingAvailable()`, `MP4_MIME`, `KEYFRAME_INTERVAL_FRAMES` (60).

**What it provides.**
- **Streaming.** Given a `fileHandle` it writes through a mediabunny
  `StreamTarget` at bounded memory instead of buffering the whole `.mp4` in an
  `ArrayBufferTarget` (which risks the renderer's ~4 GB heap cap).
- **moov-at-front while streaming.** Callers know the frame count, so it passes
  `fastStart: 'reserve'` (+ `maximumPacketCount` in the track metadata, which
  that mode requires) to reserve the sample table, stream `mdat`, then seek back
  and write `moov` ahead of it. **mediabunny defaults `fastStart` to `'in-memory'`
  for a BufferTarget but to `false` (trailing moov) for a StreamTarget**, so it is
  always passed explicitly; `tests/e2e/video-encode-streaming.mjs` asserts the
  byte order so a regression to a non-seekable file can't pass silently.
- **Real backpressure that can fail.** `await source.add(...)` settles when the
  encoder has room and **rejects** when it died, so a dead encoder cannot hang
  the export.

**Contracts it depends on** (none compile-checked — re-verify on any mediabunny
bump, see `lib/mediabunny/PROVENANCE.txt`): `keyFrameInterval` is in **seconds**,
so the historical "keyframe every 60 frames" is `60/fps`, belt-and-braced with a
per-frame `{keyFrame}` flag that mediabunny ORs with the interval; `finalize()`
and `cancel()` both **close the target's WritableStream themselves**, so a
cancelled streamed export commits a partial file.

**Deliberate scope.** H.264 only, the callers' own bitrate maths, and the
60-frame keyframe cadence. VP9/AV1 fallback is a one-line
change here but is a separate decision.

**Imports from project modules.** None. Imports `mediabunny` (importmap).

**Imported by.** `ui/overlay-export-modal.js`, `ui/export-modals.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/video-encode-streaming.mjs` drives it directly with a
stand-in file handle (headless Chromium rejects `showSaveFilePicker()`, so the
streaming path is unreachable through either modal under automation). The
modals' e2e exports (`tests/e2e/overlay-export-modal.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/export-3d-video.mjs`) cover the buffered path.

---

### ui/overlays.js

**Purpose.** Pure canvas-rendering helpers for skeleton overlays, color
palettes, and per-frame draw routines. Receives `frameGroup` and
`instanceGroups` already resolved by the caller — no project imports.

**Key exports.**
- Node markers: `drawNodeShape(ctx, x, y, shape, size, color)` — one keypoint
  marker in one of six styles (`'circle'`, `'x'`, `'triangle'`, `'square'`,
  `'diamond'`, `'cross'`; the last two for SLEAP parity in the overlay-video
  export, issue #190). All 2D node draws route through it: `drawSkeleton` and
  `drawReprojectedSkeleton` (via `options.nodeShape`, reprojection default
  `'x'`; reprojection edges honour `options.lineStyle`, falling back to a
  `[4, 4]` dash only when no style is passed) and `drawUnlinkedInstances`
  (`instNodeShape`). `drawFrameOverlays` passes the per-type Node Style as
  `nodeShape`.
- Independent node/edge visibility: `drawSkeleton` and
  `drawReprojectedSkeleton` accept `options.showNodes` / `options.showEdges`,
  **defaulting to `true`**. Only the overlay-video export sets them; they ride
  in on the per-type opts bags.
- Identity-mode name labels: `drawFrameOverlays` sections 3a (predicted) and
  4a (user) give an instance with no resolved identity its SKELETON's base
  color in `colorByIndex` (left empty, `drawInstanceLabels` would fall back to
  Track mode's vivid `getTrackColor(trackIdx)` while the skeleton is muted).
  Covered by `tests/e2e/unidentified-track-palette.mjs`.
- Color: `TRACK_COLORS` (Track mode's vivid palette),
  `UNIDENTIFIED_TRACK_COLORS` (11 muted earth tones, Identity mode's: when
  coloring by Identity a track color is never an identity color, see CLAUDE.md
  UI Conventions, enforced by `tests/test-track-identity-palette.mjs`),
  `REPROJECTION_COLOR`, `UNGROUPED_USER_COLOR`,
  `NULL_ID_COLOR` (space gray `#a7adba` for explicit-none instances when
  coloring by identity), `getTrackColor(trackIdx, colorByIdentity?)` (picks
  the palette by the mode; `getGroupColor` / `getInstanceColor` pass their
  `useIdentity` through, so an identity-less track is muted in Identity mode),
  `getGroupColor`,
  `getInstanceColor`, `adjustColorBrightness`, `errorColor`, `hexToRgb`,
  `brightenColor`, `desaturateColor`, `complementaryColor`.
  **`unlinkedDupIndices(unlinkedInstances, typeFilter)`** and
  **`unlinkedBaseColor(instance, dupIdx, session, cameraName, useIdentity,
  frameIdx)`** are the rule `drawUnlinkedInstances` colors an ungrouped
  instance by — `getInstanceColor`, darkened by `max(0.35, 1 − 0.22·k)` for the
  k-th later instance of the same type sharing a raw `trackIdx` — exported so
  the node tooltip's stripe (`ui/interaction.js` → `ui/node-tooltip.js`) uses
  the same function and cannot name a different color from the one drawn.
  `getGroupColor`/`getInstanceColor` return `NULL_ID_COLOR` when
  `useIdentity` and `session.isExplicitNoIdentity(...)`, and — when coloring by
  track — for anything on the "No ID" track (`session.isNoIdTrack(trackIdx)`).
  In identity mode `getInstanceColor` also honors a TRACKLESS unlinked
  instance's retained `Instance.identityId` (luc3d #201, stamped by
  `unlinkGroup`) before falling back to `UNGROUPED_USER_COLOR`.
  **`getGroupColor` identity resolution (issues #155, #168) — order matters:**
  1. The per-frame map keyed by the group's LIVE `trackIdx`
     (`getIdentityForTrack`) FIRST; `group.identityId` is only a fallback, since
     it is refreshed only on the frame an identity is (re)assigned and would
     paint a stale identity after a swap fix propagated forward. The
     explicit-no-id sentinel is checked AFTER that fallback, so it never
     overrides a validly-assigned group identity.
  2. A per-camera `trackIdx` is only ever queried paired with its OWN camera. If
     `cameraName` is given AND the group has a real instance there, that pair is
     the ONLY candidate — never falling through to a sibling camera's identity.
     Only with no `cameraName` (the 3D-viewport color callbacks in
     `pose/initialization.js` / `ui/export-modals.js`) or no real instance in it
     (a reprojection) does it try every member camera with a real instance, in
     `group.instances` order.
  3. It NEVER calls `getIdentityForTrack` without a `cameraName`: that falls back
     to matching the trackIdx NUMBER on any camera, which can hit an unrelated
     animal.
  Tests: `tests/test-overlays.js` "getGroupColor identity path across cameras
  (issue #168)".
  **First-frame Track-color collision (2D viewer):** when the raw per-camera
  tracker gives two DIFFERENT animals the SAME trackIdx on one frame (usually
  frame 0), `commitTrackedFrame`'s `writtenThisFrame` guard (`pose/tracker.js`)
  marks that (frame, cam, rawTrackIdx) `-1` in `frameIdentityMap` (`-1` is
  written nowhere else). When the group's own `(camera, trackIdx)` is
  `isExplicitNoIdentity` for this frame, the Track-color path falls back to the
  group's `identityId`, which is never shared between the colliding groups.
  Test: `tests/e2e/first-frame-viewer-color-collision.mjs`.
- Geometry: `videoToCanvas`, `makeVideoToCanvasTransform`,
  `computeLabelOffset`, `getLineDashPattern`, `resolveLabelDisplayScale`,
  `uprightLabelRadians`.
- **`drawLegend` is an EXPORT-only path.** The live app passes `showLegend:
  false` and shows the key as pane DOM (`ui/view-legend.js`), because anything
  on this canvas rotates with the view and anchors to the video box. The option
  and `drawLegend` stay for the overlay-video export, which has no DOM.
- **Labels stay upright when the view is rotated** (issue #162).
  `options.labelRotation` (DEGREES, via `drawFrameOverlays`'s `geoOpts`)
  reaches `drawSkeleton`, `drawInstanceLabels` and `drawUnlinkedInstances` and
  drives two things:
  - `uprightLabelRadians(options)` -> the module-private `beginUprightFrame`,
    a SCREEN-aligned frame around each anchor that cancels the view's CSS
    rotation (`applyZoom`, invisible to the canvas transform). The video and
    skeleton must STAY rotated, so cancelling per label is the only option.
    Covers node names, the track/identity pill (`drawNamePill` — plate AND
    text) and the unlinked "?" badge.
  - `computeLabelOffset`'s 7th argument, carrying the largest-gap bisector into
    SCREEN space; without it labels point into the skeleton at most angles.
  `beginUprightFrame` touches the transform ONLY when there is a rotation to
  cancel (paired with `uprightOriginX`/`uprightOriginY`), so unrotated views
  and every export path draw unchanged. Covered by `tests/test-labels.js` and
  `tests/e2e/label-upright-on-rotation.mjs`.
- **Label sizing is screen-relative, and rotation-independent.**
  `resolveLabelDisplayScale(ctx, canvasWidth, options)` = backing-store px per
  CSS px; the three label drawers multiply `options.labelSize` by it. Markers
  and edges are deliberately NOT scaled this way (video-relative, pinned to the
  animal); a name is chrome. It prefers `options.labelDisplayScale` (from
  `ui/rendering.js`: layout width × zoom). The fallback,
  `canvasWidth / getBoundingClientRect().width`, is for callers with no view
  geometry (the export modals) and is wrong on a rotated canvas (the rect is an
  axis-aligned bounding box). Covered by `tests/test-labels.js` and
  `tests/e2e/label-size-rotation-invariant.mjs`.
- Skeleton drawing: `drawSkeleton`, `drawReprojectedSkeleton`,
  `drawReprojectionErrors`, `drawSelectionHighlight`,
  `drawHoverHighlight`, `drawDragPreview`, `drawInstanceLabels`,
  `drawInstanceTypeIndicator`, `drawUnlinkedInstances`, `drawViewNameLabel`.
  **`drawViewNameLabel(ctx, text, options)`** draws a camera name **top-left**
  for "Export Video Overlays" ▸ Layers ▸ **Render Video Names**
  (`layers.videoNames`, **default ON**: the dock's name chips are never
  encoded, so an unlabelled composition is anonymous rectangles). Top-left so
  it coincides with the dock's chip, and clear of `drawLegend` (top-right);
  `options.corner: 'bottom-left'` is retained. The font scales off `ctx.canvas`
  (the tile is drawn at preview and output size; `drawLegend`'s fixed 28px is
  deliberately not copied). It resets to the identity transform and
  `filter:'none'` itself; call it OUTSIDE any view transform.
  **`drawUnlinkedInstances` "?" badge is suppressible:** it is an **editing
  affordance**, so `options.showUnlinkedBadge` (**default `true`** — a missing
  option must never strip it) turns it off while KEEPING the dashed edges and
  reduced opacity (hiding the badge must never hide the detection).
  `drawFrameOverlays` spreads it into `unlinkedOpts`, so **one flag covers both
  type passes**. Set by the Visibility panel's `visUnlinkedBadge` and pinned
  `false` by `overlayOptionsFrom` for export. Covered by
  `tests/test-unlinked-badge.js` (incl. nothing outside the badge disc changes)
  and `tests/e2e/unlinked-badge-toggle.mjs`.
  **`drawUnlinkedInstances` same-trackIdx collisions:** an unlinked instance
  colors purely by its raw `trackIdx` (no group identity to fall back to), so
  two in one (camera, frame) sharing it would be identical. Each draw call
  indexes occurrences per trackIdx among the instances drawn together and
  darkens each later one (`adjustColorBrightness`, floored at 0.35 so it never
  reaches black). Test: `tests/e2e/unlinked-instance-color-collision.mjs`.
  **`drawUnlinkedInstances` draws the track/identity NAME pill**, so the name
  survives an ungroup (the ID-correction workflow, luc3d #201: ungroup, fix a
  view, regroup). It shares the module-private **`drawNamePill(ctx, cp, text,
  color, fontSize)`** with `drawInstanceLabels`, so linked and unlinked put the
  SAME pill at the SAME anchor; it save/restores `textAlign`/`textBaseline`
  (the unlinked caller interleaves center- and left-aligned text). Gating
  mirrors the linked passes: user instances follow show-labels (4a), predicted
  only in ID mode (3a); the export inherits it via `userOpts.showLabels`.
- **`getInstanceLabelName(instance, session, cameraName, useIdentity, frameIdx)`**
  (exported) — the TEXT counterpart of `getInstanceColor`, resolved in the same
  order so the two never name different animals: ID mode + tracked → the
  per-frame identity; ID mode + TRACKLESS → the retained `Instance.identityId`
  (luc3d #201); otherwise the track name (incl. `'Track N'`). Returns `null`
  only when there is nothing to name, and the caller draws no pill. An explicit
  per-frame "no identity" falls through to the track name, as on the linked path.
- **`resolveLabelIdentity(session, instance, group, cameraName, frameIdx)`**
  (module-private) — a LINKED instance's label identity, in `getGroupColor`'s
  two steps: the per-frame map, then the group's `identityId`. Step 2 matters
  for **every group made with the Group button** (`createGroupFromUnlinked`
  sets `identityId` without writing the map). Used by passes 3a and 4a.
- **`drawInstanceLabels` `options.nameByIndex` / `options.colorByIndex`** —
  per-instance names/colors, indexed like `instances`, taking precedence over
  the trackIdx-keyed `trackNames`/`trackColors`, which cannot express two
  instances in one view sharing a trackIdx, nor trackless ones (one `null` key).
  Tests: `tests/test-unlinked-track-label.js`.
- Node trails (issue #102): `drawNodeTrails(ctx, viewName, session, frameIdx,
  options)` — mirrors SLEAP's TrackTrailOverlay. The window is the last
  `options.trailLength`+1 **present** frames up to `frameIdx` (sparse-aware, as
  SLEAP), reading only frames already in `session.frameGroups` (no lazy fetch).
  **Trails attach to what is DISPLAYED**: `options.trailUser` /
  `trailPredicted` / `trailReprojected` (from the User / Predictions /
  Reprojections boxes; omitted = on), drawn back to front like the skeletons
  (`drawTrailLayer` once per set):
  - **2D trails** follow a per-view `trackIdx` through displayed User and
    Predicted instances, linked AND unlinked (`trailViewInstances`). ONE trail
    per track across both types, so a prediction corrected into a user instance
    does not break it; the user instance wins where both exist.
  - **Reprojection trails** follow a 3D group: `groupTrailKey(group, session,
    viewName, frameIdx)` links per-frame groups (per-frame identity resolved
    like `getGroupColor`, else `identityId`, else a member's track).
    `groupReprojection(group, viewName, camera)` gives the positions the
    reprojection skeleton would: cached reprojected `Instance`, else
    `group.reprojections`, else the 3D points through `camera.project` +
    `distortPoint` (the usual case behind the playhead, as sweeps and eviction
    drop the caches). **Read-only:** it never writes those caches (the info
    panel reads them as "triangulated") and never re-triangulates. Colored like
    the reprojection skeleton (`getGroupColor` × `options.reprojBrightness`).
  Every track in the window gets a trail, including ones **vanished** from the
  current frame (it fades out). **Width:** the newest segment equals its
  layer's edge width (`options.lineWidth` / `predictedLineWidth` /
  `reprojLineWidth`, by the instance at the older end) and tapers to `1/(W-1)`
  of it, floored at 0.8 px but never above the edge width. Each segment is
  colored by the instance's color AT that frame, so an identity **switch shows
  as a color change along the trail**. Covered by `tests/test-node-trails.mjs`
  §13/§14, `tests/e2e/node-trail-width.mjs`, `tests/e2e/trails-per-layer.mjs`.
  **Shade.** `options.trailShade` — a key of `TRAIL_SHADES` (Very dark / Dark /
  Normal / Light / Very light, `amount` −0.65 / −0.4 / 0 / 0.4 / 0.7), default
  and fallback `DEFAULT_TRAIL_SHADE` ('dark', SLEAP 1.4's Trail Shade).
  `trailColor(hex, shadeKey, age)`: a negative amount scales RGB by
  `1 + amount`, a positive one blends toward white (`brightenColor`; SLEAP's
  ×1.25 does nothing to a channel at 255), and `age` (0 newest → ~1 oldest)
  moves the color further the SAME way, so a trail never fades back toward its
  track color. **Normal reproduces the pre-shade colors exactly.**
  `trailShadeFor(key)` returns the entry or null (strict; callers apply the
  fallback).
  `drawFrameOverlays` calls it right after the canvas clear when
  `options.trailLength > 0`. Length comes from **Tracks ▸ Node Trails** or the
  toolbar's **Trails** button (`state.trailSeconds` → `state.trailLength` =
  seconds × fps; see `ui/trail-presets.js`).
  **Performance (runs per view, per playback redraw):**
  - `trailWindowFrames(frameGroups, frameIdx, trailLength)` (exported) **walks
    back** from `frameIdx` (~`trailLength` lookups) instead of scanning and
    sorting every frame; after `frameGroups.size` steps without filling the
    window (sparse project) it falls back to the scan.
  - **On a LAZY project a missing frame is not an unlabelled one.**
    `drawNodeTrails` passes `residentOnly` (`!!session.lazyLoader`) and the
    window ENDS at the first non-resident frame: a seek hydrates its target and
    the frames ahead, so skipping would join trails to frames resident from
    before the jump. The draw path fills the window first
    (`ensureLazyTrailWindow`, `pose/triangulation.js`). EAGER projects keep
    SLEAP's sparse semantics. Covered by `tests/test-node-trails.mjs` §11 and
    `tests/e2e/lazy-trail-window.mjs`.
  - Segments are **batched per age step** (same style at window index k), one
    `stroke()` per step. Visible consequence: overlapping same-age segments of
    one track do not double their alpha.
  - Benchmark: `tests/e2e/_bench-playback.mjs` `SCENARIOS=...,trails<N>,...`.
- Composite: `drawFrameOverlays(ctx, viewName, frameGroup,
  instanceGroups, session, options)` — the main per-view draw entrypoint.
  `options.trackingExcluded` (from `isCameraTracked`) washes everything drawn
  for the view grey via `source-atop` (before the legend), marking a
  Tracking-Wizard-excluded view. In identity mode it also labels **predicted**
  instances with their identity (step 3a), so tracker output is proofreadable.
  Threads `trailLength` / `trailShade`, the `showUser` / `showPredicted` /
  `showReprojected` layer switches, the per-type `lineWidth`s and
  `reprojOpts.brightness` through to `drawNodeTrails`. Covered by
  `tests/test-node-trails.mjs` §12 and `tests/e2e/node-trail-shade.mjs`.
  **`options.overlaySizeScale` (issue #200).** `nodeSize` and `lineWidth` are
  in BACKING-store pixels, which do not track zoom exactly (see `ui/rendering.js`
  ▸ overlay backing-store budget), so `drawAllOverlays` passes the ratio it
  used and `makeRenderOpts` — the single chokepoint for `userRender` /
  `predictedRender` / `reprojRender` — multiplies both by it, as do the four
  `drawNodeTrails` sizes. `reprojBrightness` is not a size. **Absent or `1`
  leaves every size byte-for-byte unchanged** (the export path relies on it).
  The defaults (4 / 2) are applied here, or an unset option would escape scaling.
  **Both reprojection paths share one node color (luc3d #209).** Step 2 draws a
  reprojection from a materialized `reprojectedInstances` `Instance`, or — when
  that Map is empty, as after a bulk `triangulateAllFrames` sweep — straight
  from `group.reprojections` via `drawReprojectedSkeleton`. `reprojXColor`
  (from `options.reprojNodeColor`) and `isSelected` are computed ABOVE that
  `if`/`else` so both branches honour the Visibility panel. Test:
  `tests/e2e/reprojection-fallback-color-setting.mjs`.
- Misc: `drawLegend`, `getFrameStats`.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `ui/timeline.js`,
`ui/rendering.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`, `ui/app-state.js` (`DEFAULT_TRAIL_SHADE`),
`ui/ui-wiring.js` (`TRAIL_SHADES`, `TRACK_COLORS`, `trailShadeFor`,
`trailColor` — the trail menus), `ui/overlay-export-modal.js`,
`ui/interaction.js` (`getGroupColor`, `unlinkedDupIndices`, `unlinkedBaseColor`
— the node tooltip's color), `ui/export-modals.js` (`getTrackColor`,
`getGroupColor`), `ui/view-legend.js` (`TRACK_COLORS`, `REPROJECTION_COLOR`),
and `ui/plane-overlays.js` / `ui/plane-definition.js` /
`ui/id-switch-highlight.js` (`makeVideoToCanvasTransform`).

**User-facing features.** All on-canvas pose drawing — colored skeletons,
reprojection error vectors, drag preview, selection highlight, instance
labels, occluded/null markers.

---

### ui/rendering.js

**Purpose.** Per-frame multi-view overlay rendering pipeline. Glues
`overlays.js` draw routines to the live `state` + `triangulation.js`
data sources. Plus visibility-toggle helpers and frame counter updates.

**Key exports.**
- `setReprojErrorVisible(visible, opts?)` — show/hide the reproj-error info
  column. Showing it ticks the Reprojections and Errors boxes unless
  `opts.checkBoxes === false`.
- `DERIVED_FRAMES_KEEP` (600), `noteDerivedFrame(session, frameIdx)` — the
  reprojection fill's cap on frames with derived state (see the lazy
  reprojection fill under `drawAllOverlays`); exported for tests.
- `showReprojectionsOnly()` -> `boolean` — after Triangulate All: User,
  Predictions, Errors off, Reprojections on, each changed box firing its own `change`
  event (so the deselect-hidden-instance handler and redraw run as for a
  click); returns whether anything changed. `REPROJ_ONLY_NOTE` is the status
  suffix the callers append when it did. The four Triangulate All endings
  (`triangulateAllFrames` windowed + in-memory, `groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll`,
  `groupByTrackAndTriangulateAll`) call `setReprojErrorVisible(true, {checkBoxes:
  false})` then this, so a run is compared with the USER's boxes, not with
  Errors just re-ticked.
- `showPredictedOnly()` -> `boolean` — the tracking counterpart: after Track
  Frame / Track Frame Range / Track All (`pose/tracker.js`) Predictions on; User,
  Reprojections, Errors off, by the same change-event mechanics (shared private
  `setToolbarLayers`). `PREDICTED_ONLY_NOTE` is its status suffix.
- `getVisibilitySettings()` — reads per-view checkbox state from the DOM.
  Includes **`showUnlinkedBadge`** (the Visibility panel's *Unlinked Instances ▸
  Show "?" badge* toggle, `#visUnlinkedBadge`), passed straight through to
  `drawFrameOverlays` in `drawAllOverlays`. Read via `checkVal(id, fallback)`,
  which **defaults `true` when the element is absent**: the headless runners
  build a partial DOM, and a bare `.checked` on a missing node throws and takes
  the whole render down. Registered in `ui-wiring.js`'s `visCheckIds` (persisted;
  an older stored blob without the key keeps the HTML default) and in the
  checkbox change-listener list; it needs no deselect sweep, since it changes
  only what is painted.
  Each of `userOpts` / `predictedOpts` / `reprojOpts` carries a `nodeStyle`
  (`'circle'`/`'x'`/`'triangle'`/`'square'`) from the per-section Node Style
  button group (`visUserNodeStyle` / `visPredNodeStyle` / `visReprojNodeStyle`).
  Defaults: user `'circle'`, predicted `'x'`, reproj `'circle'` (matching the 3D
  viewer marker). `drawReprojectedSkeleton`'s own fallback stays `'x'` for
  direct callers; the user-facing default comes from here.
- `drawAllOverlays(frameIdx, viewFrames?)` — main per-frame redraw across every
  DOCKED view (`isViewDocked`: a view whose pane is closed, or that another
  solo'd camera hides, has a detached overlay canvas and is skipped — docking it
  again creates a new canvas and redraws, so nothing stale shows; with no view
  docked at all, every view is drawn, as `VideoController._shownViews` falls
  back). Optional `viewFrames` (`{ viewName: frameIdx }`, passed by the
  per-refresh playback loop in `loading/video.js`) draws each view's overlay at
  the frame ITS canvas shows (from that view's captured `VideoFrame`), so
  overlay and video agree per camera even when cameras are a frame or two
  apart; a view whose frame isn't hydrated (lazy) falls back to `frameIdx`, and
  the selection highlight only shows where the selected group exists on that
  view's frame. The lazy reprojection fill is
  `fillLazyReprojections(fi, groups)`, run for `frameIdx` and once for each
  other per-view frame — exported for the overlay video export, which calls it
  for every frame it draws (`ui/overlay-export-modal.js`). Threads
  `state.colorByIdentity`, `state.trailLength` and `state.trailShade` into each
  `drawFrameOverlays` call. With trails on in a lazy project it first calls
  `ensureLazyTrailWindow(frameIdx, state.trailLength)` (`pose/triangulation.js`),
  so the trail drawn right after a seek has the frames BEHIND the target, which
  the seek's own hydration does not load (a worker-backed loader's frames arrive
  later and trigger one redraw; a no-op during playback). It also computes the
  per-view **`labelDisplayScale`** (backing-store px per on-screen CSS px) for
  label sizing: the real `targetW` over `overlayCanvas.offsetWidth` (the LAYOUT
  width, untouched by CSS transforms) times `view.zoom.scale`. The zoom factor
  must stay in the denominator or labels grow with zoom; taking the numerator
  from `targetW` makes labels self-correcting under the overlay budget below, so
  they need no `overlaySizeScale` term. Deliberately **not**
  `getBoundingClientRect()`: `applyZoom` rotates the `.canvas-wrapper`, and a
  rect is the rotated element's bounding box, so labels would shrink at 45°.
  See `ui/overlays.js` ▸ `resolveLabelDisplayScale`.
  It passes **`labelRotation`** = `Math.round(view.rotation)`, the angle labels
  cancel to read horizontally. Rounded because the Shift+R+Arrow chord advances
  `view.rotation` fractionally per animation frame while the upright-label
  repaint is triggered off this same rounded value changing (`ui/ui-wiring.js`),
  so the two must agree.
  It passes **`showLegend: false`** unconditionally and calls
  **`syncViewLegends`** (`ui/view-legend.js`) after the per-view loop instead:
  the Display Legend key is pane DOM outside the rotating `.canvas-wrapper`, so
  it stays upright and anchored to the VIEW; driving it from here inherits the
  overlays' redraw triggers. **Playback throttle:** `updateFrameInfo` and
  `timeline.setCurrentFrame` are coalesced to ~10 Hz (`AUX_UPDATE_MS`) while
  `state.isPlaying` (the info panel is a per-frame cost capping playback fps);
  paused, they run every call, and `VideoController.stopPlayback` fires one final
  unthrottled `drawAllOverlays` so panel/playhead settle on the exact stop frame.

  **Overlay backing-store budget.** The overlay canvas is sized `videoW * ss`
  where `ss` is an effective supersample, NOT the zoom scale: at the zoom scale
  it grows as **zoom²** per view while the cell shows only a cell-sized window
  (an 8-camera session at zoom 10 allocated ~4.2 GB and dragged at 18.5 fps
  instead of 120). Rules:
  - **`ss` is bounded by what the display can RESOLVE**, not by the zoom:
    `ss = clamp(OVERLAY_QUALITY * cssW * zs / videoW, 1, zs)`. The lower bound
    of 1 keeps an unzoomed view unchanged.
  - **`ss` is QUANTIZED to 1/4 steps, rounded UP.** It depends on `cssW`, so
    without quantization a pane SASH DRAG reallocates the backing store on every
    redraw; rounding up keeps quality never below what `OVERLAY_QUALITY` asks.
  - **`OVERLAY_QUALITY` has a floor of 2** (`Math.max(2, devicePixelRatio)`):
    vector node/edge smoothness comes from this ratio, and `devicePixelRatio`
    alone made circles visibly chunkier on non-retina displays when zoomed.
  - **Sizes in BACKING pixels must be compensated.** `drawAllOverlays` passes
    **`overlaySizeScale = ss / zs`** and `ui/overlays.js` scales `nodeSize` /
    `lineWidth` by it; omit that and every node and edge grows linearly with
    zoom. `labelDisplayScale` needs no such term (see above), and
    `drawHoverHighlight` scales by its own `toCanvas.scale`.

  `MAX_OVERLAY_DIM` / `MAX_OVERLAY_AREA` then clamp `ss` to Chrome's canvas
  limits (16384 px per side, 2^28 px of area) — a **correctness** guard: past
  them the allocation reports the requested size and every draw silently
  no-ops, so the overlay goes blank with no error.
  Covered by `tests/e2e/zoom-overlay-budget.mjs`; `tests/e2e/_diag-zoom-drag-perf.mjs`
  is the headed fps harness.

  **Lazy reprojection fill — honors the group's triangulation method.** A group
  with `points3d` but no `reprojections`/`reprojectedInstances` is re-solved here
  and the result written into `state.triangulationResults` (what the Info
  Panel's error rows read). The re-solve passes
  `{ method: group.triangulationMethod === 'ba' ? 'ba' : 'dlt' }` (the same rule
  as `reTriangulateGroup`) and carries `method` into the stored result. Omitting
  it falls back SILENTLY to DLT, and since the windowed sweep
  (`sweepTriangulateAllFrames`) drops `reprojections` and
  `state.triangulationResults` project-wide, this fill is the ONLY thing that
  repopulates them — DLT's error would show under a "Bundle Adjustment" label.
  `points3d` is deliberately **not** written back (the group holds the
  authoritative 3D); `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-ba-display.mjs` asserts the two
  are bit-identical.

  **It reprojects the stored 3D instead of re-solving when that 3D is current**
  — `reprojectStoredPoints3d` (`pose/triangulation-core.js`) when the group has a
  `triangulationMethod`, is not `dirty`, and no reprojection-error threshold is
  set (with one, the solve's error leaves out observations the stored 3D does
  not record). Same numbers, ~20x cheaper than re-solving Refined — and no bulk
  triangulation path keeps derived state, so this runs for every frame shown
  after one. **3D read from a file** (`isPoints3dFromFile`: a loaded `.slp`'s,
  an imported points3d `.h5`'s) is reprojected whenever the group is clean,
  threshold or not, method or not — a re-solve would show LUCID's answer
  instead of the 3D the project holds. **Smoothed 3D** (`isPoints3dSmoothed`)
  is reprojected the same way, or the overlay would re-solve the unsmoothed
  answer (`tests/e2e/temporal-smoothing-display.mjs`). Anything else (Track
  All's provisional 3D, an edited group) is re-solved. The `.slp` load, moving a view between
  sessions and the overlay video export also leave derived state to this fill
  (`tests/e2e/load-export-move-bounded-derived-state.mjs`).

  **What it makes is kept for at most `DERIVED_FRAMES_KEEP` (600) frames per
  session** (`noteDerivedFrame`, an LRU in a `WeakMap` by session): beyond that,
  the least recently filled frame's state is dropped
  (`dropDerivedReprojections`, `pose/lazy-residency.js`) and re-derived if
  shown again — otherwise playing the whole recording would rebuild the
  derived state Triangulate All deliberately does not keep. Never dropped: the
  frame on screen, and a frame holding a `dirty` group — an edited group keeps
  showing its old reprojections until re-triangulated
  (`pose/initialization.js`), and a re-derive would replace them with a fresh
  solve. Covered by `tests/e2e/triangulate-all-bounded-derived-state.mjs` and
  `tests/e2e/bulk-triangulation-bounded-derived-state.mjs`.

  This fill stays **outside** the collapsed-info-panel gate (see
  `ui/panel-visibility.js`): the canvas reprojection markers, the 3D viewport
  and the `.slp` export all read the `_grp.reprojections` /
  `reprojectedInstances` it produces, and it fires once per group, so skipping
  it for a hidden panel would blank the canvas AND permanently deny the panel
  its numbers. The gate lives in `updateFrameInfo`, where the panel consumes
  `state.triangulationResults`.
- `updateFrameCounters()` — the status bar's Camera / Labeled Frames /
  Instances / Triangulated, over the **WHOLE project**, lazy ones included.
  Never `session.frameGroups` alone, which on a lazy project is the resident
  window. The counting rules and the whole-project baseline live in
  `ui/frame-counters.js`; this function owns WHICH camera
  (`interactionManager.lastInteractedView`, else the first view), WHEN to
  (re)build the baseline, and the DOM. Per call it is O(resident frames) on a
  lazy project (`countFrameCounters`: resident frames live, the rest from the
  cached baseline) and O(1) in project size on an EAGER one (re-counts the drawn
  frame into the baseline with `refreshCountedFrame`). A finished rebuild of an
  eager baseline drops the other views' halves rather than comparing them.
  Skipped during playback (`updateStatusBarForFrame`, `ui/info-panel.js`).

  The baseline is cached per session in a `WeakMap` (so a closed project is
  never retained), one camera half per view visited. Building it costs ~100 ms
  on a large project, so:
  - **Synchronous** only when there is none: the first update of a session
    (or after its `lazyLoader` changes) and the first time each view becomes
    active (`computeLazyCameraBaseline`).
  - Otherwise **rebuilt in the background** (`runCounterRebuild`), 250 ms after
    the last update that asked, in 8,192-frame slices via
    `createFrameCounterBaselineBuilder`. An update asks when it redraws the
    SAME frame as the previous one (or a rebuild is still pending): every data
    change (edit, Track All, Triangulate All, session-wide delete) redraws the
    current frame, so the counts follow all of them with no per-operation
    invalidation (Track All does not even mark the project dirty), while a
    redraw on a NEW frame is navigation and costs nothing. A rebuild in flight
    sets `again` instead of restarting, so updates cannot starve it. The other
    views' halves are carried over UNLESS the rebuild shows this view's
    non-resident part moved (`nonResidentCameraCounts`) — a bulk operation just
    happened — then they are dropped and recomputed on next activation.
  - An edit to the current frame shows immediately — resident frames are always
    counted live.

  **Plane placements draw last.** After `drawFrameOverlays` returns for a view,
  the loop calls `drawPlaneOverlays(view)` (`ui/plane-overlays.js`, via its
  `ui/plane-definition.js` re-export) on the same canvas — `drawFrameOverlays`
  opens with a `clearRect`, so planes drawn before it would be wiped. Runs on
  every frame and in every mode (planes are static scene geometry). Pinned by
  `tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs`.

  **The Group menu button and Temporal Smoothing are synced here too.**
  `#tbGroupMenu` (the hover menu holding Group / Edit Group) is `.active` while
  either item is, and `syncSmoothingButton()` (`ui/smoothing-button.js`)
  re-derives the Temporal Smoothing button's blue state on every draw.

  **Defining Plane Mode's toolbar lock is re-asserted here.** `drawAllOverlays`
  RECOMPUTES `tbGroup.disabled` / `tbEditGroup.disabled` from the pose selection
  on every draw, so `applyPlaneModeToolbarLock()` (`ui/plane-toolbar-lock.js`,
  via the same re-export) is called right after, not only at `enterPlaneMode`.
  A no-op when the mode is off. Pinned by
  `tests/e2e/plane-mode-toolbar-lock.mjs` §3.

  **The toolbar's Animals field catches up with a session change here too.**
  Nothing announces a session change (the switch and every load path assign
  `state.session` themselves), but each ends in a draw, so
  `syncAnimalCountField()` (`ui/animal-count-field.js`) is called right after
  the lock.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./app-state.js` — `state`, `interactionManager`, `timeline`, `isViewDocked`.
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `ensureLazyFrameData`, `ensureLazyTrailWindow`,
  `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`, `triangulateAndReproject`,
  `reprojectStoredPoints3d`, `storeReprojectedInstances`.
- `../pose/lazy-residency.js` — `dropDerivedReprojections`.
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `points3dNodeCount`, `isPoints3dFromFile`, `isPoints3dSmoothed`.
- `./overlays.js` — `drawFrameOverlays`.
- `./view-legend.js` — `syncViewLegends`.
- `./settings.js` — `isCameraTracked` (passed to `drawFrameOverlays` as
  `trackingExcluded` so views excluded in the Tracking Wizard render grey),
  `getTrackingThreshold` (the reprojection fill's threshold check).
- `./identity-assignment.js` — `editGroupState`, `finishEditGroup`.
- `./info-panel.js` — `updateFrameInfo`.
- `./frame-counters.js` — `computeFrameCounterBaseline`,
  `computeLazyCameraBaseline`, `createFrameCounterBaselineBuilder`,
  `countFrameCounters`, `nonResidentCameraCounts`, `refreshCountedFrame`.
- `./plane-definition.js` — `drawPlaneOverlays`, `applyPlaneModeToolbarLock`.
- `./smoothing-button.js` — `syncSmoothingButton` (every draw).
- `./run-buttons.js` — `syncRunButtons` (every draw).
  **Circular** (that module imports `drawAllOverlays` back); safe because both
  call sites are inside function bodies.
- `./animal-count-field.js` — `syncAnimalCountField`. **Circular** through
  `pose/tracker.js`, which imports this module; safe for the same reason.
- `./node-tooltip.js` — `syncNodeTooltipFrame`, first thing in
  `drawAllOverlays`: a node tooltip about another frame names a node that has
  moved out from under the pointer.

**Imported by.** `pose/triangulation.js`, `pose/tracker.js`,
`pose/initialization.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `loading/session-loader.js`,
`ui/identity-assignment.js`, `ui/export-modals.js`,
`ui/sessions-panes.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/plane-definition.js`,
`ui/view-align-modal.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`, `ui/overlay-export-modal.js`,
`ui/settings-modal.js`, `ui/plane-angle.js`, `ui/origin-rebase.js`.

**User-facing features.** Every overlay redraw — after seek, drag,
re-triangulate, identity assignment, or visibility-toggle change.

---

### ui/plane-dialog.js

**Purpose.** The small blocking-error / confirmation modal the plane and origin
flows share.

Split out of `ui/plane-definition.js`; it touches no plane state, no model and
no viewport. It creates `#planeDialog` (`.plane-confirm-overlay` /
`.plane-confirm-modal`, `#planeDialogMessage`, `#btnPlaneDialogCancel` /
`#btnPlaneDialogConfirm` / `#btnPlaneDialogDismiss`) on demand.

A **LEAF module**: it imports nothing.

**Key exports.**
- `showPlaneDialog({title, message, confirmLabel?, onConfirm?})` — OK-only when
  there is no `onConfirm`, Cancel/confirm when there is. **Esc CANCELS**, per the
  project modal rule, so an accidental dismissal can never apply the thing the
  user was still deciding about.

**Imported by** `ui/plane-definition.js` (which also re-exports it, so the name
stays reachable at its original path) and `ui/plane-nodes-panel.js`;
`ui/plane-angle.js` and `ui/origin-definition.js` reach it through the
`ui/plane-definition.js` re-export.

### ui/animal-count-field.js

**Purpose.** The toolbar's **Animals** field (`#tbNumAnimals`), in the Track
menu (hover **Track ▾**) below its run items: shows and edits the ACTIVE session's animal count, the number every
tracking run caps identities at (`Session.numAnimals`, through
`pose/tracker.js`). Blank is
auto-detect (`placeholder="auto"`), as in the first-use dialog
(`ui/track-setup-modal.js`) and the Track Frame Range dialog.

**Two ways the shown value goes stale, two refreshes.** A change to the count
(the first-use dialog, the Track Frame Range dialog, the field itself) repaints it through
`onTrackerNumAnimalsChange`. A change of ACTIVE SESSION has no event, so
`syncAnimalCountField()` is called from `drawAllOverlays` (`ui/rendering.js`),
which every session change ends in (two comparisons when nothing changed). It
never rewrites the field while it has focus.

**Editing.** Commits on `change` (Enter or blur), so a half-typed "1" on the way
to "12" is never the count; Enter blurs (handing the shortcuts back — a number
field blocks them all while focused, `ui/keyboard-target.js`), Esc reverts and
blurs. A bad entry — below 1, not a whole number, or text `value` reports as
`''` (`validity.badInput`, a stray "e") — reverts with an error status rather
than being read as blank, and changes nothing. With no session loaded it reverts
too and says why: the count is saved with a session. A good one goes through
`setTrackerNumAnimals`, which marks the project dirty.

**Placement and width.** BEFORE Track Frame, not after Track All, where the
label read as "Track All Animals". Not in Defining Plane Mode's toolbar lock: it
changes no annotation. The toolbar must fit a 1440px window
(`tests/e2e/toolbar-3d-toggle-button.mjs` §7), so the field is compact (11px
label, 26px input). The Trails menu (`#trailsMenu`) hangs from its button's
RIGHT edge, or the closed menu runs past the window as scroll overflow.

**Key exports.**
- `wireAnimalCountField()` — called once from `setupUI` (`ui/ui-wiring.js`).
- `syncAnimalCountField()` — called from `drawAllOverlays`; O(1).

**Imports from project modules.** `./app-state.js` (`state`),
`../import-export/save-load.js` (`setStatus`), `../pose/tracker.js`
(`getTrackerNumAnimals`, `setTrackerNumAnimals`, `onTrackerNumAnimalsChange`).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/rendering.js`.

Covered by `tests/e2e/toolbar-animal-count.mjs` and, for the file format, `tests/e2e/visibility-settings-roundtrip.mjs`
and `tests/test-visibility-metadata.js`.

### ui/plane-toolbar-lock.js

**Purpose.** Disable the pose-annotation toolbar while Defining Plane Mode is
on, and restore it exactly as it was on exit.

Split out of `ui/plane-definition.js`. Self-contained: it reads one flag
(`planeState.active`) and otherwise only touches toolbar DOM, with no fan-in
from the rest of the panel.

**Key exports.**
- `applyPlaneModeToolbarLock()` — **idempotent, and must stay so**, because
  `drawAllOverlays` re-derives `tbGroup.disabled` / `tbEditGroup.disabled` from
  the pose selection on every overlay draw and the mode redraws constantly. Each
  button's PRIOR `disabled` and `title` are snapshotted on the transition only —
  re-snapshotting on the re-assert would record the LOCKED state and make the
  restore a no-op.

Adding a button to the lock means adding its id to `PLANE_LOCKED_TOOLBAR_IDS`;
if it opens a menu, its wrapper also needs `PLANE_LOCKED_DROPDOWN_IDS`, since a
`.tri-dropdown` menu opens on hover and its items are `div`s, so `disabled` on
the button reaches neither. Locked menus: Instance and Group (actions only).
The Track / Triangulate / Smooth menus are NOT wrapper-locked:
they also hold settings that stay live in the mode (Method, Animals,
Settings…), so they open, and each run item is a `<button>` in
`PLANE_LOCKED_TOOLBAR_IDS` (a new run item there must be too; Smooth's
Remove/Restore smoothing toggle, `tbSmoothToggle`, is one). A disabled
button still opens its menu on hover (`tests/e2e/plane-mode-toolbar-lock.mjs`
§4b).

**Imports** `planeState` from `ui/plane-definition.js` — circular, and safe only
because the binding is read INSIDE the function body. **Imported by**
`ui/plane-definition.js` (re-exported) and, through that path,
`ui/rendering.js`.

### ui/plane-overlays.js

**Purpose.** Draw the placed planes onto a view's 2D overlay canvas.

Split out of `ui/plane-definition.js`. Pure rendering: it reads the model and
the interaction state and writes pixels, and nothing in the panel calls back
into it.

**Key exports.**
- `drawPlaneOverlays(view)` — called from `drawAllOverlays` AFTER
  `drawFrameOverlays`, which begins with a `clearRect`, so drawing earlier would
  be wiped. Planes are drawn in **every** mode, not just Defining Plane Mode —
  they are geometry the user annotated, and hiding them outside the mode would
  make them look lost. Only the SELECTION and HOVER decorations are mode-gated.
  Fills and edges are per PLANE; NODES are drawn once each over the union, since
  a corner two planes share is one node with one 2D point.

**What is drawn outside the mode is the user's to choose**, through the
Visibility panel's `Planes` section: `planeVisibility`
(`ui/plane-visibility.js`) gates the plane BODY (fill, edges, plane name) and
the NODES separately, and forces every part on while the mode is active. It
decides WHETHER a plane is drawn, never HOW — the fill still comes from
`plane.filled` alone, so a shown plane looks the same in both modes.

**Imported by** `ui/plane-definition.js` (re-exported, which is how
`ui/rendering.js` still reaches it unchanged). **Imports** `planeVisibility`
from `ui/plane-visibility.js` beside the hub bindings.

### ui/plane-visibility.js

**Purpose.** Which PARTS of the annotated planes are drawn, in which
representation — the one reader of the Visibility panel's `Planes` section.

**Key exports.**
- `PLANE_VIS_IDS` — the four checkbox ids (`visPlanes2D`, `visPlanes3D`,
  `visPlaneNodes2D`, `visPlaneNodes3D`), keyed by the field each produces.
  Exported so `ui/ui-wiring.js`'s `visCheckIds` persistence list and the tests
  name them from here: a toggle missing from that list still works and silently
  forgets, which is invisible until the next reload.
- `planeVisibility(modeActive)` → `{planes2d, planes3d, nodes2d, nodes3d}`.

Three things it decides, and each has a reason:
- **Defining Plane Mode overrides all four.** The panel's tables and the 2D/3D
  drags act on parts the user has to see, so honouring a toggle inside the mode
  would hide the thing being edited.
- **They decide WHETHER a plane is drawn, never HOW.** A shown plane keeps its
  own colour, edges and `Fill`. There is deliberately no fill override:
  `plane.filled` is project state that only the `Fill` button sets. The toggles
  ride `localStorage.visibilitySettings` (browser-local, never the `.slp`, so
  `save-golden-digest.mjs` cannot move).
- **A missing checkbox reads as ON.** The headless runners build a partial DOM,
  and a harness that silently renders nothing and passes is far harder to spot
  than a stray plane.

**NO IMPORTS, deliberately** — it is read from inside the plane panel's
`plane-definition` ↔ `plane-overlays` cycle and from `ui/ui-wiring.js`, and a
module with no edges of its own cannot be the one that closes a loop.
**Imported by** `ui/plane-overlays.js` (2D), `ui/plane-definition.js`
(`syncPlanes3D`) and `ui/ui-wiring.js` (the ids, for persistence and the
change listeners). Covered by `tests/e2e/plane-visibility-toggles.mjs`.

### ui/plane-nodes-panel.js

**Purpose.** Section 1 of the Define Planes panel: the global Nodes table, the
padlock (pin) picker, the typed x/y/z editor and node deletion.

Split out of `ui/plane-definition.js`; the pin popover singleton is read and
written nowhere else in the app.

**The model state stays in the hub.** `planeState`, and the one
`new PlaneModel()` on it, is imported and never redeclared: a second copy would
diverge silently, since the tests assert behavior rather than object identity.

**Key exports.**
- `renderNodesTable()` — one row per pool node; rebuilt on almost every
  interaction, which is why selecting a row must NOT rebuild it (see
  `setSelectedNode` in the hub).
- `renderPinInfoButton()` — wired ONCE, guarded by `dataset.wired`; it is not a
  per-repaint renderer despite the name.
- `renderFrozenWarning()`, `planeLockWhere(lock)` — the latter is shared with
  the hub's 3D corner drag, which reports where a plane-locked node was pulled
  back to.

The pin picker REPOSITIONS on scroll rather than dismissing: a `scroll` event
arrives a frame late, so dismissing made it impossible to open on a row you had
just scrolled to.

**Imports** `state`, `setInfoTip`, `showPlaneDialog`, `setStatus`/`markDirty`,
`PIN_STATES`, `nodeFreezeState`, `reprojectPointCamera`, `isOriginModeActive`,
and from the hub `ICON_PIN`/`ICON_INFO`/`makeDeleteButton`/`setEmptyState`/
`redraw`/`planeModel`/`planePool`/`planeState`/`refreshPlanePanel`/
`refreshTriangulationErrors`/`syncPlanes3D`/`syncSelectedNode3D`. Every hub
binding is read inside a function body only.

### ui/plane-editor-panel.js

**Purpose.** The EDITOR half of the panel's **Planes** section: the name
field, the member list, the "+ Add" existing-node picker and the edge pickers,
for whichever plane is selected. It renders directly under the roster
(`ui/plane-list-panel.js`) inside the same `<details>`.

**One section, two modules.** The roster and the editor are one
`#planePlanesDetails` in the markup, split by what they render: the roster
draws EVERY plane, this draws the SELECTED one.

**It does not CHOOSE the plane.** There is no plane dropdown: a second writer
of `planeState.selectedPlaneId` would duplicate what the Planes table owns
(row click selects; `+ New Plane`, `#btnNewPlaneSkeleton`, creates and
selects — as in **3D Mesh Objects**). `renderEditor` reads `getSelectedPlane()`
and writes no selection state at all.

**Key exports.**
- `renderEditor()` — the module's ONLY export and its entry point, called by
  the hub's `refreshPlanePanel`. It also calls into the Nodes section
  (`renderNodesTable` / `renderFrozenWarning`); that is one-directional, as
  that module never calls back.

**Imported by** `ui/plane-definition.js`.

### ui/plane-list-panel.js

**Purpose.** The ROSTER half of the panel's **Planes** section: the Planes
table, its action row, the `Views: annotated / total` fraction and the per-plane
placements sub-row. The table is also the DRAG SOURCE that places a plane onto a
video view, and **the one control that SELECTS** which plane the editor below it
(`ui/plane-editor-panel.js`, same `<details>`) edits.

Cohesive by what it renders, not by size: everything here draws or wires one
Planes row, and nothing here solves anything — Triangulate and Fit are reported
through the hub's `triangulatePlaneAndReport` / `fitPlaneAndReport`.

**Key exports.**
- `renderPlanesTable()`, `renderActionRow()`, `renderOriginButton()`.
- `ICON_TRIANGULATE` / `ICON_MESH` / `ICON_FIT` — the action glyphs, also set by
  the hub's one-time wiring.
- `annotatedViewStats(plane)` stays private to the module but is the reason it
  exists: it counts **hand-placed** views only, never reprojected ones, because
  `Triangulate` reprojects a plane into views it was never placed on and
  counting those would make the fraction read full after one Triangulate and
  never say anything again. It uses the SAME test `triangulatePlane` applies to
  choose contributing views, so the number and the solver cannot disagree.
  Pinned by `tests/e2e/plane-views-column.mjs`.

**Imported by** `ui/plane-definition.js`.

### ui/plane-definition.js

> **This module was SPLIT.** The panel's parts and three cross-cutting helpers
> live beside it:
> `ui/plane-nodes-panel.js` (Nodes), `ui/plane-list-panel.js` (the Planes
> roster) and `ui/plane-editor-panel.js` (the selected plane's editor, under
> that roster in the same section), plus
> `ui/plane-dialog.js`, `ui/plane-toolbar-lock.js` and `ui/plane-overlays.js`.
> The hub kept the model accessors, the placements, the two solver controllers
> (triangulate and fit), the mode lifecycle, `refreshPlanePanel`, the
> interaction callbacks and the one-time wiring — the orchestration that drives
> the sections.
>
> **`planeState` did not move, and must not.** It is declared here and holds the
> one `new PlaneModel()`; a second copy would diverge silently, since the tests
> assert behavior rather than object identity.
>
> **The export surface is part of the contract** — every name importable from
> this path before the split still is, several by re-export, because ~25 of
> them are reached by `await import('/ui/plane-definition.js')` from the e2e
> tests. `redraw`, `makeDeleteButton`, `setEmptyState`, `ICON_PIN`,
> `ICON_INFO`, `syncSelectedNode3D`, `planeImmutableMask` are exported for the
> split-out modules.
>
> Each split-out module imports its hub bindings CIRCULARLY and reads them
> inside function bodies only — never at module top level, where a circular
> import is still `undefined`. Preserve that when adding to any of these files.


**Purpose.** "Defining Plane Mode" (**View ▸ Define Planes**) — the annotation
UI for re-defining the 3D viewer's origin. The end goal of that work is a
**translation vector + rotation matrix** that move the world frame onto a plane
the user marks up; this module owns how the user *draws* that plane and owns
nothing about the solve. The data model (`PlaneNodePool`, `PlaneSkeleton`,
`PlaneInstance`, `PlaneModel`) lives in `pose/plane-nodes.js` +
`pose/plane-data.js` and is partly re-exported here.

The pool, the planes and the origin frame live in app-session memory; the
per-view 2D lives on the active `Session`. **All of it is persisted** by
`import-export/plane-metadata.js`, so **every plane/node MUTATION calls
`markDirty()`**. The rule is per mutation, **not** per repaint:
`refreshPlanePanel()` / `syncPlanes3D()` / `redraw()` run for plenty of reasons
that change nothing on disk, and marking from them would leave the dot
permanently on. The node-size / edge-width / 3D-corner-size sliders are the
deliberate exception — browser-local display taste, so they must not mark it
dirty.

**Nodes are GLOBAL and a node may be in several planes.** A plane is an
ordered list of pool node IDs plus optional edges, so two planes MEET along a
shared line: its corners are one node with one 3D position and one 2D point
per view, and re-solving either plane cannot split the line apart.

**The panel's SHAPE is that model made visible: two SIBLING sections**, not a
nodes editor nested inside a plane editor — a node outlives the planes that
reference it.

1. **Nodes** (`#planeNodesDetails`) — the project-wide pool, one row per node;
   expanding reveals its 3D position and the planes using it. Everything in it
   acts on the NODE (every plane using it); there is **no membership column**.
   Carries `#planeNodeNameInput` + `#btnAddPlaneNode`, `#planeNodesEmpty` and
   `#planeFrozenWarning`. **`+ Node` mints a POOL node and touches no plane**;
   a duplicate name is refused rather than minting a second node under a name
   the user identifies the first by. A node in zero planes is a valid resting
   state (`.plane-node-unused`). The × here **destroys** the node project-wide
   and confirms first when it is shared or pinned, naming the planes it will
   change.
2. **Planes** (`#planePlanesDetails`) — the roster AND the selected plane, in
   this order. Its summary is `Planes` plus the selected plane's name
   (`#planeEditorTitle`), so a folded section still says which plane it holds.

   First the **roster**: `#planeSkeletonsTable` (the drag source, with
   per-plane expander and placement rows; each row's meta reads
   `N off, N reprojected`, since a reprojected view is otherwise
   indistinguishable from a user-placed one) and `#planeSkeletonsEmpty`.
   **This table is the only selector**: a row click is the one writer of
   `planeState.selectedPlaneId` besides `createPlane`.

   Then **`+ New Plane`** (`#btnNewPlaneSkeleton`, in
   `.panel-button-row.plane-create-row`), OUTSIDE `#planeEditorContent` so it
   stays live with nothing selected — with no planes it is the only control in
   the section that can do anything. It creates, and the creation selects.

   Then the **selected plane**, `#planeEditorContent`, swapped for
   `#planeEditorEmpty` when nothing is selected: the EDITABLE name
   `#planeSkeletonName` (the one place a plane is renamed), then three
   foldables. `#planeMembersDetails`: a members table (`#planeMembersTable`,
   `NAME | COLOR swatch | ×`) whose × is
   `removeNodeFromPlane(…, {deleteIfOrphan:false})` — the REFERENCE goes, the
   node keeps its 3D, pin and 2D — plus the **add-an-existing-node** control
   (`#planeAddNodeSelect` + `#btnAddExistingPlaneNode`), the **only** way a
   node enters a plane and how an intersection is built (pick a node another
   plane already uses); disabled with a spoken reason when no node is left.
   `#planeEdgesDetails` (edges are per-plane and OPTIONAL — triangulation and
   fitting ignore them; they are visual reference and can state a CONCAVE fill
   outline; without them the fill hulls the points). `#planeActionsDetails`
   (**Actions**): `.plane-action-row` (Triangulate / Fill / Fit), `Set Origin`
   and `Set Angle Between Planes`.

   Last, outside `#planeEditorContent`: `#planeAppearanceDetails` (**Plane
   Appearance**) — it styles every plane, so it must not empty-state with the
   editor.

**Planes has ONE labelled row, and it says `Name`**, as 3D Mesh Objects does.
`.plane-name-row > span` is a FIXED 72px column (`flex: 0 0 72px` + `nowrap`)
so the two sections' Name fields line up. `tests/e2e/plane-section-info.mjs`
§6 pins one such row, no plane dropdown, and `+ New Plane` above the field;
`tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs` §2 asserts the section body's whole child
list.

**Member node names are INDENTED** (12px on `#planeMembersTable`'s first
column, header included), because `.plane-node-shared` paints its 2px accent
bar INSIDE the row. §7 of the same test pins it.

**Entering the mode mints NO plane.** `enterPlaneMode` re-selects an existing
plane but creates nothing: a phantom `plane_1` is indistinguishable from one
the user made and forgot. Pinned by `define-plane-mode.mjs` §1b.

**Key exports.**
- `planeState` — `{ active, model, selectedPlaneId, selectedNodeId, nodeSize,
  edgeWidth, nodeSize3d, expanded, expandedNodes }`. `active` is the mode flag;
  `model` is the `PlaneModel` (pool + planes + per-view 2D);
  `nodeSize`/`edgeWidth`/`nodeSize3d` are ONE shared value each across every
  plane (Plane Appearance sliders); `expanded` is the set of plane ids whose
  placement dropdown is open. `selectedNodeId` is the pool node the Nodes table
  has SELECTED and the 3D view is ringing — transient, never saved, and
  deliberately separate from `expandedNodes` (expanding asks to READ a node's
  coordinates, selecting asks to be SHOWN where it is).
- `planeModel()` — the model, with its 2D **bound to the active session**:
  the placements map lives on the `Session` and is adopted through
  `attachPlacements` whenever the active session changes (identity check, so
  the re-sync/prune runs once per switch). Every accessor below goes through
  this.
- `planePool()` / `getPlanes()` / `getPlane(id)` / `getSelectedPlane()` /
  `createPlane(name)` / `deletePlane(id)`. **Deleting a plane keeps every one
  of its nodes** — auto-pruning would throw away a pinned coordinate or a
  positioned 2D point as a side effect. The Nodes table is the one place a node
  is destroyed.
- `planePoints3d(plane)` / `planeNodeNameAt(plane, idx)` / `planeHasAny3d(plane)`
  — the plane-ordered views of pool state that `ui/origin-definition.js` and the
  3D payload read.
- `getPlaneInstance(view)` / `getPlaneInstances(view)` / `placedPlanesOn(view)`
  / `placedViewsOf(plane)` / `placePlaneOnView(...)` / `unplacePlaneFromView(...)`
  — ONE `PlaneInstance` per VIEW, indexed by pool order, on
  `session.planePlacements` (`Map<viewName, PlaneInstance>`). Deliberately
  **not** keyed by frame: a plane is static scene geometry. Un-placing KEEPS
  the 2D, so re-placing restores exactly what the user positioned.
- `triangulatePlane(plane)` — solves the plane's 3D corners across every view it
  is placed on, writing them onto the NODES (`writePoints3dForPlane`, which
  refuses pinned ones) plus `plane.triangulation`. Like
  `triangulateAndReproject`, observations are **undistorted** before the DLT
  and error is measured in each camera's **native** pixel space against the raw
  annotation. Right-click-disabled nodes are excluded per view. Pinned nodes
  keep their stored 3D (`mergeFrozenPoints3d`) but their residual IS measured
  and reported separately (`summarizePlaneTriangulation`) — "your pinned anchor
  no longer agrees with where you clicked". DLT only, no BA. Returns
  `{ok:false, reason}` rather than throwing.
  - **Requires 2+ HAND-PLACED views, not 2+ placements.** Reprojected views ARE
    placed but contribute nothing, so a placement count would let a plane
    annotated on ONE view return an answer built from one ray. The gate counts
    views with at least one usable (positioned, not nulled, not derived)
    observation, and `plane.triangulation.views` lists only those — otherwise
    `refreshTriangulationErrors` would average in a zero-by-construction view.
  - Then it calls `reprojectPlaneIntoUnplacedViews`, and
    `triangulatePlaneAndReport` **redraws the 2D overlays** — triangulation
    writes 2D, so without that the new placement stays invisible until some
    unrelated repaint.
- `reprojectPlaneIntoUnplacedViews(plane)` — writes the plane's solved 3D into
  the `PlaneInstance` of every view it is NOT placed on, flags those points
  `derived`, and places the plane there — into the real instance rather than a
  read-only overlay so the user can grab a corner there and fix it (dragging
  is what turns it into evidence). Three refusals:
  - **Never claims to be evidence** — every written point is `derived`, so it is
    out of the next solve and out of the error average (see `PlaneInstance`).
  - **Never writes a mirrored ghost** — a point BEHIND a camera still divides
    through to a plausible pixel (`reprojectPoint` does not check the sign of
    `w`), so each node is gated on `cameraDepth > 0`; a view with nothing in
    front is left alone and reported in `behindViews`.
  - **Never overwrites a view the user placed** — except points that are
    themselves derived, which are REFRESHED everywhere, since a stale
    reprojection reads as current.
  Off-frame reprojections in front of the camera are still written (clamping
  would fake a corner). Cameras with no `state.views` entry are skipped.
  Un-placing a reprojected view is undone by the next Triangulate, by design.
- `planPlaneFit(plane)` / `applyPlaneFit(plane, plan)` / `fitPlane(plane, opts)`
  — the **second half** of the origin pipeline, split so the outcomes that
  need the user happen before anything is written. With **no pinned node** it
  is `fitPlaneToPoints3d` + `projectPoints3dOntoPlane`; with one or more,
  `fitPlaneConstrained` + `projectPoints3dOntoPlaneConstrained`. The free case
  is deliberately NOT routed through the constrained solver (it would move
  floats for no benefit; that solver returns `not_constrained` to stop it). The
  correction goes to both `syncPlanes3D` and, via `reprojectPointCamera`, every
  placed view's 2D — **skipping pinned nodes** (their annotation is the
  user's). Nodes toggled OFF still get their 2D updated, flags preserved.
  Derived points are rewritten too but excluded from the reported `movedPx`
  ("how far did the fit move YOUR annotations") and from
  `refreshTriangulationErrors`.
  **`applyPlaneFit` stores the fit BEFORE the write, and the write goes through
  the `constrain` hook.** `planeImmutableMask` reports only `locked`, so
  without the hook fitting one plane drags a plane-locked corner it shares with
  another plane off that other plane. ORDER: a plane-locked corner of the plane
  being fitted is held by that plane too, so a hook run before `plane.planeFit`
  is replaced projects onto the superseded plane, leaving the corner off the
  plane just fitted (`tests/e2e/plane-lock-holds.mjs` §4).
- **Blocking vs confirming.** `fitPlaneAndReport` dispatches on the result
  **CODE**, never on the message text. `no_anchor_3d` / `anchors_collinear` /
  `anchors_noncoplanar` / `underdetermined` BLOCK: the message goes to a dialog
  and the status bar and **nothing is mutated**. The `mutable_far_from_plane`
  warning CONFIRMS instead (the fit is valid; only flattening a corner metres
  onto it is drastic).
- **Stale-fit propagation.** After a successful fit the nodes that actually
  MOVED are found by an `Object.is` diff of input vs output (exact: immutable
  entries are bit-identical, missing ones stay NaN), and
  `planesInvalidatedByFit` names the OTHER planes standing on them. Their
  `planeFit` is dropped and the user is told which went stale.
- `syncPlanes3D()` — pushes every plane that has 3D into `viewport3d.setPlanes`
  (`points3dForPlane` / `planeEdgesLocal` / `planeFillOrder3d` /
  `planeNodeColors`, all in the plane's own node order). `polygonOrder` is the
  FILL's ring (edge cycle or convex hull), not membership order. Full rebuild,
  so it is also the REMOVE path; no-op when `viewport3d` is null. Also the one
  place the 3D drag callbacks are wired (idempotent — the viewport is
  re-created per session load), and the one place the Visibility `Planes`
  toggles reach 3D (`viewport3d.showPlaneSurfaces` / `showPlaneNodes` from
  `planeVisibility(planeState.active)`); the payload is unchanged by them —
  every plane with 3D stays in it, because `_planes` is what a drag, the node
  marker and the mesh highlight resolve ids against. The payload's `editable`
  is `planeState.active && !isOriginModeActive() && !isAngleModalOpen() &&
  !!model.usableFitForPlane(plane)` — a **USABLE** fit, not a stored one,
  since pinning any node clears the stored fit. `isAngleModalOpen` is the 3D
  half of the Set Angle edit lock (see `ui/plane-angle.js`). The payload carries
  the usable fit as `planeFit` (the drag surface) and `fitted: !!plane.planeFit`
  separately, because **Set Origin's node picker asks "has the user DECLARED
  this plane's frame?"** (`fittedPlanes()`), and widening the drag surface must
  not make an un-Fit plane eligible to define the origin. It also carries
  `nodeIds` (parallel to `nodeColors`) so the viewport can resolve the SELECTED
  pool node against corners it draws per plane. It then calls the private
  `syncMeshObject3D()`, which pushes the SELECTED 3D Mesh Object's member plane
  IDS into `viewport3d.setMeshMembership` (or `null`) — here because every
  plane mutation routes through this function. **IDs, never geometry:** the
  derived geometry is in the user's ORIGIN frame while the viewport group is in
  CALIBRATION world.
- **Selecting a node** — `syncSelectedNode3D()` (exported, for
  `ui/plane-nodes-panel.js`) / `setSelectedNode(id)` /
  `applyNodeSelectionClass()` / `onDocumentClickForNodeSelection(e)` (the
  last three private). `syncSelectedNode3D` pushes `planeState.selectedNodeId`
  into `viewport3d.setSelectedPlaneNode` as an ID and is called at the end of
  `syncPlanes3D`, so a rebuild cannot leave the marker behind.
  `onDocumentClickForNodeSelection` is ONE `click` listener on `document`, in
  the CAPTURE phase, wired once in `setupPlaneDefinition`: anything carrying
  `data-plane-node-id` (the Nodes table's two rows, a plane's per-node
  triangulation lines) selects that node, everything else clears it. Capture,
  because row controls `stopPropagation` (swatch, padlock, pin popover) and a
  click on one still means "this node"; one listener, so no surface can be
  forgotten and hold a stale marker. `setSelectedNode` does NOT rebuild the
  table (it toggles `plane-node-selected` on existing rows), because the
  selecting click often focuses the name field and a rebuild would discard the
  input. A stale id is dropped in `renderNodesTable`, which every node-deleting
  route ends in.
- **3D corner dragging** — `onPlaneNodeDragged3D` / `onPlaneNodeDragEnd3D`
  (private; installed by `syncPlanes3D`). The viewport has already constrained
  the position to the dragged plane's surface; these write it onto the node,
  then `reprojectPointCamera` into every placed view. **The 2D follows the 3D
  here** (and in the typed editor) — the reverse of the 2D edit paths: a fitted corner is *defined*
  by the plane. Per-move work is `syncPlanes3D` + `redraw`; the panel catches up
  on drag end. Two pin states:
  - A **LOCKED** node is refused (once per drag, reason in the status bar): the
    payload marks draggability per PLANE, so the pin is enforced here.
  - A **PLANE-LOCKED** node is **held** — the position goes through
    `constrainPoint3dForNode`, which gets the last word (the viewport only knows
    the plane under the cursor, so a shared corner could otherwise be pulled
    off its other planes). `onPlaneNodeDragEnd3D` names what holds it (a plane,
    the line two meet in, the point three meet at); a corner in three planes
    cannot move, and the drag says so.
  The gate is `usableFitForPlane`, matching the payload, or a corner advertises
  a drag that then does nothing.
- **The Nodes list** — `renderNodesTable` / `renderNodeDetailRow` /
  `renderPinInfoButton`, the pin picker and the typed-position editor all live
  in `ui/plane-nodes-panel.js`. A LIST of nodes: colour leads (it identifies
  the corner on every view and in 3D), the name is a borderless input,
  coordinates are one chevron away, the pin is a padlock. `Pinned` carries
  `#planePinInfo`, defining the THREE states. The delete × is a bare grey glyph,
  not the shared red `.panel-btn`.
- **The pin picker** — `openPinPopover` / `closePinPopover` /
  `positionPinPopover` / `onPinPopoverReflow` / `schedulePinPopoverClose` /
  `setNodePin`, and `ICON_PIN` (which stays in the hub). One 15px padlock per
  row, coloured by state; hovering or clicking floats a three-state picker.
  - **It floats** (`position: fixed`, appended to `document.body`): in-row it
    would need three icons' width, and `.plane-panel`'s `overflow-y: auto`
    would clip it at the first and last row.
  - **A scroll REPOSITIONS it; it only closes when the icon leaves the panel**
    (a `scroll` event arrives a frame late). `define-plane-mode.mjs` §19.
  - **Hover opens, a click makes it sticky**, leaving closes after a 260ms
    grace; Esc and an outside mousedown close it too.
  - **The current state is shown, dimmed and `disabled`.**
  - **`setNodePin` is model logic, not widget logic**: Set Origin Mode refuses;
    `plane-locked` is refused for a node in no plane; every plane standing on
    the node loses its stored fit (a pin change changes what a fit may do). The
    status names what holds the node, asked AFTER the change (so it answers
    with derived planes, like every other reader); a member plane that cannot
    yet say where it is is still NAMED, with the reason.
- **Typing a position** — `commitNodeXyz` / `applyTypedNodePoint` /
  `reprojectNodeIntoPlacedViews` / `fmtXyz` (all private). The keyboard path to
  what the 3D drag does: **the 2D follows the 3D** (every placed view rewritten
  to the reprojection), per-node errors re-derived, stored `planeFit`s left
  alone (a corner must not move the frame it defines).
  - **In an expanded row** because three legible number fields do not fit
    beside the row's own controls in the fixed 300px panel.
  - **A field still showing what the panel printed keeps its FULL stored
    double.** Display is 4 decimals and all three fields are read per commit,
    so re-parsing would round the untouched axes — editing z would move x.
  - **Revert-on-invalid, except when there is nothing to revert to:** a node
    with no 3D is being ENTERED, so partial numbers stay until the third
    arrives.
  - **`locked` is read-only and `plane-locked` is projected.** Locked fields
    are `disabled` with no commit listener (a guard in `commitNodeXyz` covers
    the pin changing under a live field); Plane-locked goes through
    `constrainPoint3dForNode` and the status names what it was moved to.
    `planeLockWhere` is the one builder of that phrase, shared by the typed
    editor, the drag-end status, the padlock tooltip and the pin picker.
- `enterPlaneMode()` / `exitPlaneMode()` / `togglePlaneMode()` /
  `isPlaneModeActive()` — show/hide the `#planeModeBar` banner and swap the
  info panel's `.panel-tabs` + `.panel-tab-content` for `#planePanel` by inline
  `display` only, so exiting restores the previously-active tab and its scroll.
  Exiting also clears the plane selection/hover and unwinds the two things that
  lock this panel: `exitOriginMode()` and `closeAngleModal()`.
- `handlePlaneDrop(planeId, viewName, clientX, clientY)` — the drop listener's
  core. Converts the cursor with `interactionManager.canvasToVideo`, so a drop
  lands under the pointer at any zoom / pan / rotation.
- `planeInteractionCallbacks()` — the callback bag `ui/interaction.js` needs
  (`isPlaneEditMode`, `getPlaneInstances`, **`getPlaneNodeIndices`**,
  `getPlaneEdges`, `getPlaneNodeSize`, **`beginPlaneDrag`**,
  `isPlaneDataLocked`, `onPlaneChanged`, `onPlaneSelectionChanged`). Merged into the InteractionManager's callbacks in
  `pose/initialization.js` so that module keeps no import of the plane feature.
  `getPlaneNodeIndices` reports the indices actually SHOWN on a view (an
  instance covers the whole pool, so a node whose only plane is un-placed would
  otherwise be grabbable while drawing nothing). `beginPlaneDrag` refuses a
  **pinned** node, restricts an Alt+drag to the grabbed plane's nodes, and
  refuses every node while the Set Angle dialog is open (the click still
  SELECTS). `isPlaneDataLocked` (`isAngleModalOpen`) closes the lock's other
  two doors: the right-click null toggle, and — in `handlePlaneDrop` — a plane
  row dropped onto a view (a `<tr>` takes no `disabled`).
- `onPlaneChanged(inst, movedIndices, {moved})` — a 2D edit invalidates the 3D of
  **the nodes that moved**, not the whole plane. Pinned nodes are skipped
  (`invalidateNode3D`), but every plane standing on a moved node still loses
  its fit. When `moved` is true it also clears those nodes' `derived` flags — a
  point the user dragged is theirs. Deliberately NOT done inside `setPoint`:
  the fit's 2D write-back and the 3D corner drag use the same setter and must
  not promote the model's own output to evidence.
- `drawPlaneOverlays(view)` — re-exported from `ui/plane-overlays.js` (see
  that entry). Called by `drawAllOverlays` **after** `drawFrameOverlays`. Three
  node states are visually distinct because they mean different things to the
  next solve: **solid** = the user's annotation, counted; **hollow grey** =
  nulled; **ghosted with a dashed ring** = derived, shown and draggable but not
  counted. Nulled wins when both apply. A **pinned** node gets an extra white
  ring, so a refused drag reads as a lock, not a bug.
- `refreshPlanePanel()` — rebuilds the panel: `renderEditor`, then the planes
  table, action row, origin button, angle button, origin result, and finally
  `refreshMeshObjectsPanel()`. Full DOM rebuild per mutation, deliberately. It
  ends with the private `applyAngleModalLock()`, which disables every control
  in `#planePanel` while the Set Angle dialog is open — LAST, so it overrides
  the renders above, and re-asked every render rather than remembered, so
  unlocking needs no bookkeeping.
