- `setupPlaneDefinition()` — one-time wiring. Called once from
  `pose/initialization.js`. Calls `setupMeshObjects()`.

**The 3D Mesh Objects table is NOT here.** It lives in `ui/mesh-objects.js`;
this module's involvement is one import, the `setupMeshObjects()` and
`refreshMeshObjectsPanel()` calls and the private `syncMeshObject3D()`. The
objects feature is strictly additive, and keeping it out of this file keeps
that checkable.
- `PLANE_DRAG_MIME` — `'application/x-lucid-plane-skeleton'`.
- Re-exports `PlaneModel` / `PlaneSkeleton` / `PlaneInstance` / `PlaneNode` /
  `PlaneNodePool` / `seedPlanePoints` / `planePolygonOrder` /
  `points3dForPlane` / `nodeFreezeState` from `pose/plane-data.js`.

**Editing is gated on the mode.** `isPlaneModeActive()` backs the interaction
manager's `isPlaneEditMode` callback, so **outside** Defining Plane Mode a
plane draws but never takes a click — plane nodes can never compete with pose
nodes during normal annotation. Inside the mode planes take priority on
mousedown, and a MISS falls through to the normal handling, so pose editing
still works while the mode is on.

**One placement per view per plane.** A second drop of the same plane on the
same view is refused with a status warning rather than re-seeding, so carefully
positioned nodes can't be destroyed by a stray drag.

**The drag payload must never be `text/plain`.** `ui/sessions-panes.js` tells
dockview to `accept()` any drag over the dock whose types include `text/plain`
and turn the drop into a **new video panel** named from the payload. With a
private MIME dockview's `onUnhandledDragOverEvent` never accepts, and this
module's delegated listeners on `#videoDock` get the events.
`tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs` asserts `dragstart` sets *only* the private
MIME and that a real browser drag spawns no dockview panel.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/plane-data.js` — `PlaneModel`, `PlaneSkeleton`, `PlaneInstance`,
  `PlaneNode`, `PlaneNodePool`, `seedPlanePoints`, `planePolygonOrder`,
  `planeFillOrderPoolIndices`, `planeFillOrder3d`, `planeNodeIndices`,
  `planeNodeNames`,
  `planeNodeColors`, `planeNodeImmutability`, `planeEdgesLocal`,
  `planeEdgesPoolIndices`,
  `planeCentroid2d`, `points3dForPlane`, `writePoints3dForPlane`,
  `nodeErrorsForPlane`, `nodeFreezeState`.
- `../pose/plane-nodes.js` — `PIN_STATES`.
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `hasPoint3d`, `getPoint3d`.
- `./app-state.js` — `state`, `interactionManager`, `viewport3d`.
- The split-out modules: `./plane-dialog.js` (`showPlaneDialog`),
  `./plane-toolbar-lock.js` (`applyPlaneModeToolbarLock`),
  `./plane-nodes-panel.js` (`renderNodesTable`, `renderPinInfoButton`,
  `renderFrozenWarning`, `planeLockWhere`), `./plane-list-panel.js`
  (`renderActionRow`, `renderPlanesTable`, `renderOriginButton`,
  `ICON_TRIANGULATE` / `ICON_MESH` / `ICON_FIT`), `./plane-editor-panel.js`
  (`renderEditor`); `drawPlaneOverlays` is re-exported from
  `./plane-overlays.js`. All but `plane-dialog` import the
  hub back (circular, call-time only).
- `./mesh-objects.js` — `setupMeshObjects`, `refreshMeshObjectsPanel`,
  `getSelectedMeshObject` (circular).
- `./plane-angle.js` — `setupPlaneAngle`, `renderAngleButton`,
  `isAngleModalOpen`, `closeAngleModal` (circular).
- `./origin-definition.js` — the Set Origin wizard (`enterOriginMode`,
  `exitOriginMode`, `isOriginModeActive`, `attachOriginCallbacks`,
  `setupOriginDefinition`, `renderOriginResult`, `fittedPlanes`). **Circular**
  (that module imports `planeState` / `planeModel` / `getPlane` / `planePoints3d` /
  `planeNodeNameAt` / `syncPlanes3D` back); call-time use only.
- `./overlays.js` — `makeVideoToCanvasTransform`.
- `./section-state.js` — `persistSectionStates` (every collapsible section in
  the panel except Danger Zone, under the `planeSectionsOpen` key).
- `./info-tip.js` — `setInfoTip` (the Pinned column's ⓘ, the Planes table's
  Views column and the ⓘ in each major section's `<summary>`:
  `PLANE_SECTION_INFO` / `wireSectionInfoButtons`).
- `./plane-visibility.js` — `planeVisibility`, read by `syncPlanes3D` (the
  same module backs the 2D half in `ui/plane-overlays.js`).
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `setStatus`, `markDirty`.
- `./rendering.js` — `drawAllOverlays`, and `../pose/triangulation.js` —
  `triangulatePoints`, `reprojectPointCamera`, `cameraDepth`, `fitPlaneToPoints3d`,
  `projectPoints3dOntoPlane`, `fitPlaneConstrained`,
  `projectPoints3dOntoPlaneConstrained`, `mergeFrozenPoints3d`,
  `summarizePlaneTriangulation`, `planesInvalidatedByFit`. Both **circular**
  (rendering imports `drawPlaneOverlays` from here, and triangulation imports
  rendering); safe because every use is inside a function body.

**The mode blocks the pose-annotation toolbar.**
`applyPlaneModeToolbarLock()` (`ui/plane-toolbar-lock.js`, re-exported; see
that entry) disables `+ Instance` / `- Instance` / `Group` / `Edit Group` /
`Triangulate` / `Triangulate All` / `Track Frame` / `Track All` while the mode
is on: they act on a POSE selection the user can no longer see or change in
the mode. The VISIBILITY controls, Sessions, Tracks / Identity and the Panel
toggle stay live — they change what is DRAWN. The two Triangulate buttons'
`.tri-dropdown` wrappers also get `.plane-mode-locked` (`pointer-events:
none`), since their hover menus' `div` items ignore `disabled`. Each button's
prior `disabled` is snapshotted on the transition and restored on exit (as
`lockUI` in `ui/origin-definition.js` does), so a button disabled for its own
reasons (Track All mid-run) does not come back enabled.

**Imported by.** `ui/rendering.js` (`drawPlaneOverlays`,
`applyPlaneModeToolbarLock`), `ui/ui-wiring.js`
(`togglePlaneMode`, for the View menu item), `pose/initialization.js`
(`setupPlaneDefinition`, `planeInteractionCallbacks`, `refreshPlanePanel` —
called next to `syncPlanes3D` in `setup3DViewport`, which is the one place a
freshly RESTORED plane model reaches the Nodes / Planes tables on every
project-load path), `ui/origin-definition.js`, `ui/origin-rebase.js`,
`ui/plane-angle.js`, `ui/mesh-objects.js`, the split-out
`plane-nodes-panel` / `plane-list-panel` / `plane-editor-panel` /
`plane-toolbar-lock` / `plane-overlays` (circularly),
`import-export/plane-metadata.js` (`planeState`, at call time).

**DOM it owns.** `#planeModeBar` (+ `#planeModeExit`) under the toolbar, and
`#planePanel` inside `#infoPanel` — **three sibling `.info-section`s**, in this
order:
1. `#planeNodesDetails` — the POOL list `#planeNodesTable`
   (`.plane-nodes-table`), columns **(chevron) / Color / Name / Pinned /
   Delete**. The three icon columns are content-sized, so their labels set their
   width and Name takes what is left; `.th-center` centres a label and its
   control together. `Pinned`'s label carries `#planePinInfo`, the button
   explaining the three states. There is no membership column and no 3D column:
   the padlock says what the state badge used to, and the row carries the rest
   (its `title` is `nodeStateTitle`, and `plane-node-frozen-unsolved` draws an
   error stripe).

   A node's IDENTITY row is `.plane-node-main`: `.plane-node-expander` (with
   `.plane-caret`), `.plane-node-color`, `.plane-node-name`,
   `.plane-node-pin-btn[data-pin]` and `.plane-node-del`. **Select it with
   `tr.plane-node-main`, never `tbody tr`** — an expanded node adds a second
   row. That row, `.plane-node-detail`, appears only while the node is in
   `planeState.expandedNodes`, and holds one `colspan=5` cell ▸
   `.plane-node-detail-body` ▸ `.plane-node-xyz-line` (three
   `.plane-node-xyz-cell`: `.plane-node-xyz-label` +
   `.plane-node-xyz-input[data-axis]`) and `.plane-node-planes`
   (`.plane-node-plane-chip`, one per plane the node is in, with EVERY plane
   currently holding a plane-locked node marked `.plane-node-plane-holder` and
   carrying a `.plane-node-plane-mark`). Both rows carry
   `data-plane-node-id` and
   `.plane-node-row.plane-node-{mutable,frozen,frozen-unsolved}` plus
   `.plane-node-unused` / `.plane-node-shared`; the open identity row also gets
   `.plane-node-open`. Both rows of the SELECTED node carry
   `.plane-node-selected`, which tints the cells rather than adding a third
   left bar (`.plane-node-shared` and `.plane-node-frozen-unsolved` already
   claim the 2px inset). `data-plane-node-id` is what makes an element SELECT
   its node (`.plane-tri-node` lines too). The picker is
   `#planePinPopover` (`.plane-pin-popover` ▸ `.plane-pin-option[data-pin]`,
   the current one `.is-current`), a child of `document.body` rather than of any
   row. With it: `#planeNodesEmpty`, the
   `#planeFrozenWarning` line, and `#planeNodeNameInput` + `#btnAddPlaneNode`
   (pool-only: it creates no plane and joins none).
2. `#planePlanesDetails` — **Planes**. Summary =
   `.plane-summary-label` ("Planes") + `#planeEditorTitle` (the SELECTED
   plane's name) + `#planePlanesInfo`. The body, in order:
   `#planeSkeletonsTable` (the **Planes** table, drag
   source and the only selector; `data-plane-skeleton-id`, `.plane-expander`,
   `.plane-placements-row` ▸ `.plane-placements-body` ▸ `.plane-placement-item`
   / `.plane-tri-summary` / `.plane-tri-node` ▸ `.plane-tri-node-name` /
   `-xyz` / `-err` / `-pin`, each node line also carrying
   `data-plane-node-id`), `#planeSkeletonsEmpty`, then
   `.panel-button-row.plane-create-row` ▸ `#btnNewPlaneSkeleton`
   (`+ New Plane`), then `#planeEditorEmpty` and `#planeEditorContent` — the
   latter swapped for the former when nothing is selected. Inside
   `#planeEditorContent`: `#planeSkeletonName` (the editable name),
   `#planeMembersDetails` (the members
   table `#planeMembersTable`, columns **Name / Color swatch / ×**, rows
   carrying `data-plane-member-id` and `.plane-member-name` /
   `.plane-member-swatch`; `#planeMembersEmpty`; `#planeAddNodeSelect` +
   `#btnAddExistingPlaneNode` + `#planeAddNodeHint`), `#planeEdgesDetails`
   (`#planeEdgesTable`, `#planeEdgesEmpty`, `#planeEdgeSrcSelect` /
   `#planeEdgeDstSelect` / `#btnAddPlaneEdge`) and `#planeActionsDetails`
   (**Actions**: the `.plane-action-row` ▸ `#btnPlaneTriangulate` /
   `#btnPlaneFill` / `#btnPlaneFit`, then `#btnSetOrigin`, then
   `#btnSetPlaneAngle`). Last in the body and OUTSIDE
   `#planeEditorContent`: `#planeAppearanceDetails` (**Plane Appearance**:
   `#planeNodeSize` / `#planeEdgeWeight` / `#planeNodeSize3d`), a SUB-section
   that ships `open`.

The modal `#planeDialog` for blocking errors and confirmations is created by
`ui/plane-dialog.js` (see that entry). Styles live under the
"Defining Plane Mode" block at the end of `styles.css`.

**Section titles are 14px** (`.plane-details > summary`); the four
sub-sections inside Planes (Nodes In This Plane, Node Connections, Actions,
Plane Appearance) stay 12px via `.plane-subdetails`.
`tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs` §2 asserts both sizes.

**The pin states are named for the padlock.** `PIN_LABELS`
(`ui/plane-nodes-panel.js`) reads **Unlocked / Plane-locked / Locked**; the
model values (`'none'`, `'plane-locked'`, `'locked'`) and the file format are
unchanged.

**Icons are inlined SVG strings** (`ICON_PIN`, `ICON_INFO` here;
`ICON_TRIANGULATE` / `ICON_MESH` / `ICON_FIT` in `ui/plane-list-panel.js`) — no
asset requests, and `currentColor` lets one padlock take the state's colour.
**The geometry in `ICON_PIN` is the only surviving copy** of the traced
padlocks — edit it there, not from a file.

**The panel's width belongs to the tables.** `#infoPanel` is a fixed 300px, so
`.plane-panel`, its `.info-section` wrapper, `.plane-details-body` and cell
padding are trimmed, and the generic `.data-table` 120px cell cap is lifted
inside the plane panel (it would clip a row of inputs). Room is made
VERTICALLY by folding `<details>`. Node rows have no hover highlight and a
default cursor — they are forms, not clickable rows.

**`frozen-unsolved` is said in the open.** A node pinned before it was ever
triangulated can never acquire a 3D and blocks every fit of every plane it
belongs to (`no_anchor_3d`). So it gets its own badge colour AND a
`#planeFrozenWarning` line naming the nodes, not just a tooltip.

**Three shared appearance values, not two.** `planeState.nodeSize` (2D, canvas
px) and `planeState.nodeSize3d` (3D scene units) are in unrelated units, and
`nodeSize3d` is separate from the viewport's `skeletonNodeSize` so sizing plane
corners never resizes pose nodes. `wirePlaneSlider`'s optional `apply` callback
lets the 3D slider re-push the scene (`syncPlanes3D`) instead of redrawing.

**The Planes table** (rendered by `ui/plane-list-panel.js`, as is everything
through **It FITS the 300px panel** below). Columns are expander / Name / Nodes / **Views** / delete.
Expanding a row lists that plane's placements inline (one line per view, click
to select, × to un-place) plus the triangulation, pinned-anchor and plane-fit
readouts. The row is the drag handle, so the expander and action cells suspend
`tr.draggable` on `mouseenter` — otherwise a button press starts a drag.

**Views is `annotated / total`, and ANNOTATED is not PLACED** — see
`ui/plane-list-panel.js` (`annotatedViewStats`). A shared node hand-placed for
a neighbouring plane counts for this one too (one node is one 2D point per
view). With no calibration loaded the cell is a dash, not `0/0`. The column's
ⓘ (`planeViewsInfo`, in `PLANE_SECTION_INFO`) is **one short sentence**; the
reprojected-corners caveat lives on the CELL's own `title`.
`tests/e2e/plane-views-column.mjs` §5 holds the tip under 120 characters and
one sentence. Do not bring back the `+n` shared-node badge (reads as an
addition), the `3D` badge, or a placed-view count beside the caret (a third,
different view number) — see CLAUDE.md.

**It FITS the 300px panel**, or the section scrolls horizontally:
- **Fixed table layout.** An auto-layout table ignores `width: 100%`, so
  `#planeSkeletonsTable` (and `#planeMembersTable` / `#planeEdgesTable`) is
  `table-layout: fixed` with binding `<th>` widths — 20 / auto / 46 / 62 / 28 px.
  62 is the measured minimum for "VIEWS" + its ⓘ; below it the header becomes
  "VIEWS…" (`plane-views-column.mjs` §6). A long plane name ellipsizes and
  repeats in `tdName.title` (which also carries the row's drag hint, since a
  cell `title` wins). `#planeNodesTable` is NOT fixed: its inputs size
  themselves.
- **`white-space: nowrap` leaks** from `.data-table tbody td` into the sub-row,
  so its summary sentences live in `.plane-placements-body`, which undoes it
  (and adds the `margin-left: 22px` + left rule an expanded node has).
`.plane-tri-node` is a **wrapping flex row** (`.plane-tri-node-name` / `-xyz` /
`-err` / `-pin`); only the name ellipses, and the whole line is repeated in the
row's `title`. A pinned node shows the `ICON_PIN.locked` padlock, not the word.
Pinned by `define-plane-mode.mjs` §18h (a worst-case row asserting no
horizontal overflow anywhere in the panel).

**The shared action row** (`#btnPlaneTriangulate` / `#btnPlaneFill` /
`#btnPlaneFit`) acts on the **selected** plane and is disabled only when
nothing is selected; otherwise a failed precondition is reported in the status
bar on click. `renderActionRow()` owns it.

**`#btnSetOrigin`** hands off to `ui/origin-definition.js`, gated on a FITTED
plane anywhere — not on the selection, since the wizard picks from any fitted
plane in 3D. `renderOriginButton()` owns it.

**`#btnSetPlaneAngle`** hands off to `ui/plane-angle.js`, gated on **two**
planes with a USABLE fit (`anglePlanes()` — see that module).
`renderAngleButton()` (imported, called from `refreshPlanePanel`) owns it.

**Triangulate says which nodes it could not move.** `showLockedNodesDialog`
raises a modal naming a plane's `locked` nodes whenever `Triangulate` skipped
any, and `triangulatePlane` refuses outright (with `lockedNodeNames`) when
EVERY node is locked. A success-line parenthetical is not enough because **Set
Angle Between Two Planes locks nodes automatically**, so a user can hold locks
they never set. **`Fit` deliberately does NOT get this dialog:** holding
pinned nodes is what a CONSTRAINED FIT is *for*, and `reportFit` already says
how many were held.

`showPlaneDialog` (re-exported from `ui/plane-dialog.js`) and
`refreshTriangulationErrors` are exported so `ui/plane-angle.js` reuses them
rather than growing second copies.

**A major section explains itself in an ⓘ, not a paragraph** (Nodes, Node
Connections, Planes and 3D Mesh Objects; see CLAUDE.md). `PLANE_SECTION_INFO`
maps each ⓘ to its sentence and `wireSectionInfoButtons` installs them ONCE
from `setupPlaneDefinition` — they are static markup (`renderPinInfoButton` is
the exception: its icon lives in a rebuilt table header). The Danger Zone and
the Planes section's "+ Add" deliberately have neither paragraph nor ⓘ.

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs`, `tests/e2e/plane-mode-toolbar-lock.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/plane-persistence-roundtrip.mjs` (the dirty flag),
`tests/e2e/plane-angle.mjs` (the locked-node dialog) and
`tests/e2e/plane-section-info.mjs` (an ⓘ click does not fold its section).

---

### ui/origin-definition.js

**Purpose.** "Set Origin Mode" — the wizard that collects the two inputs
`pose/origin-frame.js` needs, applies the result, and reports it.

**The wizard.** Three steps, because both inputs are picked in the 3D scene and
each must be committed before the next is meaningful:

| step | action | result |
|---|---|---|
| `node` | click **any node** of a **fitted** plane | the new origin |
| `axis` | click the red (+n) or blue (−n) arrow | the new +Z |
| `confirm` | Cancel (back to `node`) / Continue | applies |

**ANY node of the plane, not just one on its outline** (a node mid-floor is
often where the physical mark is). `_handleOriginPick` collects one mesh per
`plane.nodeIds` entry, so the wizard's copy says **node**, never "corner" —
`tests/e2e/origin-picks-any-node.mjs` pins the copy and a click on an interior
node.

**Only fitted planes offer nodes, and FITTED here means the user clicked
Fit.** Enforced in the viewport via `userData.planeFitted`, fed by the
payload's `fitted` flag — independent of `planeEditable` (dragging is OFF
during the wizard, but the nodes stay pickable) and of the payload's
`planeFit`, which may be derived. A derived surface is enough to slide a corner
along, but picking the origin declares the project's frame. `fittedPlanes()`
counts stored fits only, and entering the mode is refused when nothing is
fitted, so the button, the picker and this list agree.

**Both candidate arrows are always drawn** (the choice is between a normal and
its negation). Choosing dims the loser, and the axis picker stays armed through
`confirm`, so switching is one click.

**Key exports.** `originState`, `enterOriginMode` / `exitOriginMode` /
`isOriginModeActive`, `fittedPlanes()`, `pickOriginNode(planeId, nodeIdx)` /
`pickOriginAxis('positive'|'negative')`, `cancelOriginPick` / `applyOrigin` /
`clearOrigin` / `confirmClearOrigin`, `exportUpdatedCalibration()`,
`renderWizard` / `renderOriginResult`, `setupOriginDefinition`,
`attachOriginCallbacks(vp)`.

**The UI lock.** `lockUI` walks the menu bar, toolbar, info panel, viewport and
timeline and records each button's **prior** `disabled` state, restoring it
exactly on exit — blanket-enabling would bring back buttons that were disabled
for their own reasons (the plane action row with nothing selected). Esc, the
mode's Exit button and the wizard's Cancel/Continue are the exceptions and the
only way out. The menu bar's dropdowns are `div`s, so `disabled` cannot reach
them: `body.origin-mode-lock` blocks them in CSS instead. `exitPlaneMode`
unwinds this mode first, or leaving would strand every button disabled.

**Applying an origin does not move any data.** `viewport3d.setOriginFrame` moves
the displayed grid + axes — and the ORBIT with them, so dragging and zooming
re-center on the new origin (`_rebaseControls`, see `ui/viewport3d.js`); cameras,
skeletons and planes stay in calibration world coordinates (see CLAUDE.md).

The frame **is** persisted: `import-export/plane-metadata.js` writes its two
INPUTS — the origin and the chosen +Z — and `pose/origin-frame.js` rebuilds
the rest deterministically on load, so derived quantities cannot contradict
the inputs. Applying or clearing an origin calls `markDirty()`; entering and
leaving the mode does not.

**Arrow length is scaled to the PLANE** (`arrowLengthFor`: 70% of the plane's
reach from the picked node), not to the camera baseline — a fixed length is
invisible on a room-sized plane and off-screen on a small one.

**`fittedPlanes()` asks the POINTS, not the plane.** A plane keeps its
`planeFit` until something clears it, but its corners' 3D can be invalidated
independently (they live on the nodes), so the filter requires at least one
node with a 3D position — otherwise the wizard would dead-end on a corner that
no longer exists. `pickOriginNode`'s `nodeIdx` is an index into the PLANE's own
node order (what the 3D payload was laid out in), not a pool index; the two
differ as soon as a node is shared.

**Imports from project modules.** `./app-state.js` (`state`, `viewport3d`),
`../import-export/save-load.js` (`setStatus`, `markDirty`),
`../pose/origin-frame.js` (`buildOriginFrame`, `rebaseExtrinsics`),
`../pose/pose-data.js` (`getPoint3d`, `hasPoint3d`, `Camera`),
`../import-export/file-io.js` (`exportCalibrationTOML`, `downloadTOML`),
`../loading/calibration-pick.js` (`REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME`), and
**circularly** `./plane-definition.js` (`planeState`, `planeModel`, `getPlane`,
`planePoints3d`, `planeNodeNameAt`, `syncPlanes3D`, `showPlaneDialog`) and
`./origin-rebase.js` (`showSetCalibrationModal` — this module owns the button,
that one owns what it does) — call-time use only, same rule as the other cycles
in this directory. Plane 3D lives on the shared node pool, so it is read
through `planePoints3d(plane)` and `planeNodeNameAt`, never off the plane
object.

**Imported by.** `ui/plane-definition.js`, `ui/origin-rebase.js`,
`ui/plane-nodes-panel.js` (`isOriginModeActive`), `ui/plane-list-panel.js`
(`fittedPlanes`), `ui/mesh-objects.js` (`originState`),
`import-export/plane-metadata.js` (`originState`, at call time).

**DOM it owns.** `#originModeBar` (+ `#originModeExit`), the
`#originInstruction` overlay inside `.viewport3d-container`
(`#originDragHandle`, `#originStepText`, `#originLegend`, `#originConfirmRow`
with `#btnOriginCancel` / `#btnOriginContinue`), and, in the plane panel,
`#originResultSection` / `#originResultDetails` / `#originResult` plus
`#originDangerSection` / `#originDangerDetails` (`#btnExportCalibration`,
`#btnSetCalibration`, `#btnClearOrigin`). The overlay is `pointer-events: none`
except for its button row and its drag grip, so it can never steal a pick from
the canvas below.

**The overlay is draggable** (`setupInstructionDrag` / `placeBox` /
`applyBoxPosition`), because it floats over the very node or arrow the wizard
asks the user to click. Only the grip (`#originDragHandle`) takes pointer
events, so the box never swallows picks. The position is page-session state
(module-level `boxPos`, container-relative px, not persisted), re-clamped each
time the box is shown since the viewport is resizable. Placing it explicitly
also clears the stylesheet's centring `translateX(-50%)`.

**Two collapsible blocks, not one section.** The readout lives in
`#originResultDetails` ("Defined Origin", **open** by default) and the three
actions that reach OUTSIDE the 3D view in `#originDangerDetails` ("Danger
Zone", **collapsed** by default). Both appear and disappear with
`originState.frame`: without an origin all three actions are no-ops.

- **`Export New Calibration`** → `exportUpdatedCalibration()`, which downloads
  `calibration-rebased.toml` (`REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME` from
  `loading/calibration-pick.js`, shared with `ui/origin-rebase.js` — the two
  produce byte-identical TOML for a given origin): every camera's extrinsics
  through `rebaseExtrinsics`, written with `exportCalibrationTOML` /
  `downloadTOML`. The other half of the deliverable: applying an origin moves no
  point, so downstream consumers need a calibration that agrees on where the
  world is. Intrinsics, distortion, size and camera ORDER are untouched, and the
  rotation keeps the NOTATION it arrived in (3×3 for anipose-style, Rodrigues
  otherwise), so a diff shows the origin change and nothing else. Returns the
  TOML string (what the e2e asserts on).
- **`Set as New Calibration`** → `ui/origin-rebase.js`, which writes
  `calibration-rebased.toml` (never over `calibration.toml`) AND re-bases every
  3D number in the project so the world becomes the defined origin. See that
  module's entry for the ordering that makes a cancel safe.
- **`Reset to Calibration Origin`** goes through `confirmClearOrigin`, which raises `showPlaneDialog` first. `clearOrigin`
  itself stays unguarded — the load path (`plane-metadata.js`) and the tests must
  reach it without a dialog; the confirmation is about an unrecoverable CLICK
  (nothing records which node and arrow were picked).

**The Defined Origin readout is stacked, not tabular** (`renderOriginResult` →
`vectorBlock` → `namedBlock`): each of the seven vectors is a `.origin-block`
— NAME plus a muted `.origin-unit` span, then x/y/z on an indented
`.origin-block-body` line; the 3×3 `R` takes the same shape. A five-column
table does not fit ~300px. The layout classes are the Nodes list's own
`.plane-node-xyz-line` / `-cell` / `-label`, shared so the two readouts line
up — **a coupling to keep in mind when those rules change.** The value is a
`.origin-xyz-value` span, not `.plane-node-xyz-input`: computed outputs must
not look typeable. 3 decimals throughout.

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/define-plane-mode.mjs` §14 — both blocks, the export
(the TOML round-tripped through `parseCalibrationTOML`, with a negative
control), the Reset warning (Esc/Cancel keep the frame; only Confirm clears),
and `#originResult` overflow measured on a wide-coordinate restage as well as
the fixture (the fixture alone would not pin it).
`tests/e2e/_diag-origin-panel.mjs` (not part of any suite) screenshots both
blocks to `$OUT` and `$OUT-danger.png`; `WIDE=1` stages the wide case.

---

### ui/origin-rebase.js

**Purpose.** The dialogs, the progress bar, the Cancel button and the file write
for **`Set as New Calibration`** — the committing twin of `Export New
Calibration`. The maths and the plan are `pose/origin-rebase.js`'s.

**The order of operations is not arbitrary.**

1. Confirm, with the inventory (`countRebaseTargets`).
2. **Get the file handle, still inside the click.** `showSaveFilePicker` needs
   transient user activation, which expires in a few seconds, so asking for it
   *after* a minute of re-basing throws `SecurityError`.
3. Plan — the slow part — behind a blocking progress modal with Cancel.
4. Write the file.
5. Only then swap the buffers in.

Every failure mode therefore leaves the project untouched: a cancel or a failed
write drops the plan. Step 5 is the one irreversible moment and it is
synchronous, so it cannot fail part-way.

**The calibration file is never overwritten.** The re-based calibration goes to
a NEW file, `calibration-rebased.toml` (`REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME`) — not over
the `calibration.toml` the project was annotated against, which is usually
shared with tools outside LUCID. `Export New Calibration` writes the SAME name,
because for a given origin the two produce byte-identical TOML.
`#originRebaseStaleWarning` states that the new file takes PRECEDENCE over
`calibration.toml` when a folder holds both (`pickCalibrationFile`). It
deliberately makes no claim that a folder `.toml` beats the calibration embedded
in the `.slp`: true of `handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp`, but NOT of reopening
the `project.slp`, which never scans a folder.

**The project IS saved, automatically.** `autoSaveAfterRebase` runs `quickSave`
as the commit's last step, so `project.slp` agrees with the calibration written
beside it. It saves ONLY when `state.slpFileHandle` already exists: `quickSave`
would otherwise open `showSaveFilePicker` without the transient activation it
needs, so that case gets a status asking for Save As instead. Success is read
from the DIRTY FLAG (`!state.isDirty`), not from the absence of an exception,
because `quickSave` reports its own failures without re-raising. It runs before
the summary status line, or `quickSave`'s own status text would overwrite the
counts. There is no completion modal.

**A multi-session commit leaves a THIRD consequence, stated in the
confirmation.** There is one calibration file per session FOLDER and this action
writes exactly one, so the other re-based folders still hold a calibration in
the old frame. `#originRebaseMultiNote` (when `t.sessions > 1`) names the count
and ends with the one thing left to do — *Ensure other re-based sessions have an
updated calibration file in their folders.* It deliberately does NOT explain the
old-frame/new-frame consequence or promise a load-time note
(`ui/calibration-notice.js` still reports the divergence), and it is in the
confirmation so the user can still decline. Its first clause follows the
SELECTION below (`All 2 loaded sessions are re-based…` vs `1 of 2 loaded
sessions are re-based…`). The folder count is `picked - 1`, NOT `total - 1`: a
DESELECTED session kept its old 3D and its old calibration, which still agree,
so it is not a folder to fix. When `picked === 1` the trailing clauses are
omitted entirely rather than naming zero sessions. Pinned by
`tests/e2e/set-new-calibration.mjs` §6/§7/§8.

**On a multi-session project the user CHOOSES which sessions move.**
`#originRebaseBySession` carries a checkbox per row
(`.origin-rebase-session-toggle`, `data-session-index`) and the Continue click
hands `runSetCalibration` the FILTERED array — no mode flag, so there is no
partial code path to drift. Four things about it:
- **The ACTIVE session's toggle is checked and DISABLED.** Its cameras are what
  `calibration-rebased.toml` describes, and the project-wide `PlaneModel` — the
  node pool and every plane fit — moves once, with it; leaving it behind would
  write a calibration for a frame the project's own plane geometry is not in.
  It is found by `sessions.indexOf(state.session)`, NOT by
  `state.activeSessionIdx` and not by assuming 0: `state.session` is what
  `runSetCalibration` reads to choose the file's cameras, so pinning the row by
  anything else could let the pinned row and the written calibration name
  different sessions.
- **Every headline number follows the selection**, through
  `subsetRebaseTally` — a re-FOLD of `tFull.perSession`, not a re-count, so a
  click does not re-walk a lazy project's columnar store. One `renderSelection()`
  owns all four selection-dependent strings (both tables, the block note, the
  hazard warning, the multi-session note) and is called at build time too, so no
  initial string can drift from it. `plane nodes` / `Plane fits` deliberately do
  NOT move with the selection: one project-scoped `PlaneModel`, moving once.
- **Deselecting is a real hazard and `#originRebaseSubsetWarning` says so**,
  in two sentences and ONLY while something is switched off: the sessions left
  behind keep their old 3D, so the project holds 3D in two coordinate frames —
  exactly what `pose/calibration-compare.js` detects on the next load. It sits
  under the checkboxes, as feedback on the control that causes it.
- **`runSetCalibration` force-includes `state.session`** if the caller left it
  out. Unreachable from the UI, but without it `exportCalibrationTOML` would be
  handed zero cameras and write a valid, EMPTY calibration — silently, and
  after the 3D had already moved.

**Write access.** `Load Calibration` goes through a plain `<input type=file>`
(bytes, no write handle) and a session-folder load gives no handles at all, so
the user points at the file once per page session (`calibHandle`, not
persisted; `resetCalibrationHandle()` is the test seam). Without the File System
Access API it degrades to a plain download. Only the ACTIVE session's cameras go
into the file, and the dialog says so.

**Blocking means blocking.** The progress modal's scrim stops the pointer and a
capture-phase `keydown` swallower stops the app's `document`-bound shortcuts,
which would otherwise scrub the timeline or start a triangulation mid-re-base.
Esc is passed through as Cancel.

**The inventory is TWO tables, and which table a row is in is the claim being
made about it.** `#originRebaseCounts` is *Origin-dependent — updated* (3D
points to update, split into pose keypoints and plane nodes; plane fits; camera
**extrinsics**); `#originRebaseKeeps` is *Not origin-dependent — unchanged*
(2D user/predicted instances, reprojections, plane placements, camera
**intrinsics + distortion**, image size and camera order). A camera appears in
both on purpose: "does this rewrite my intrinsics?" is the most common fear
about pressing the button. The first title says *updated*, not *rewritten*:
these numbers are re-expressed in a new frame, not overwritten.

**Those two blocks FOLD; the per-session one does not.** `buildTable`'s fifth
argument, `collapsible`, builds the block as a real `<details open>` whose
`<summary>` is the title (`summary.origin-rebase-block-title` in `styles.css`),
so the keyboard and the accessibility tree come from the browser; the note and
the table both sit inside. The **state is deliberately not persisted** — no
`persistSectionState`, nothing in `localStorage`: the two-table split is the
claim this dialog asks the user to trust, so a fold made once must never be how
the next re-base is confirmed. Every open rebuilds them expanded.
`#originRebaseBySession` stays a plain `div` because it holds the per-session
checkboxes. Still ONE scroller — the `<details>` add no `overflow`.
`buildTable` returns `{ table, note, block }`.

**Every row is present for every project, zeros included.** A per-camera
session with no `project.slp` reads 0 in its 3D rows — which is how a reader
tells an un-triangulated session from a triangulated one. Hiding them would make
the modal's shape depend on the load path.

**`#originRebaseBySession` appears only when `sessions > 1`.** Each row is a
checkbox + the session name, then `N 3D · M cam · K 2D`. It is its own block
rather than sub-rows of the totals, which already decompose by provenance and
sum exactly to their headline. The name cell carries NOTHING else (no
`(active)` suffix): the pinned row is read off its disabled control, and the
block note names the session, since a disabled input's own tooltip never fires.

**The modal caps its own height and scrolls, with the action row `position:
sticky`**, so Cancel / Continue stay reachable on a laptop with ONE scroller;
the tables never get their own.

**The 2D rows are the whole population, with the grouped subtotal beside
them** (`12,480 — 2,604 in instance groups`). Inventory, not work — 2D does not
move — but a mostly-ungrouped project must not read as holding none.

**The origin-dependent table's headline is the SUM, and every sub-row under it
is a count of POINTS.** `3D points to update` quotes `tally.points3d`; beneath
it the three pose buckets and `plane nodes` add up to it. Group counts live in
the row LABELS (`from 2 User instance groups`) so no column mixes units. The
success status line likewise names the total and then the pose/plane split.
Pinned by `tests/e2e/set-new-calibration.mjs` §1 (the sub-rows sum to the
headline) and §5 (a planes-only project).

**Key exports.** `showSetCalibrationModal()`,
`runSetCalibration(sessions)` — whose argument is the CHOSEN subset, not
necessarily every loaded session — and `resetCalibrationHandle()`. The file name itself is
`REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME`, from `loading/calibration-pick.js` — a module with
no imports, so both origin actions and the folder loaders can read it without
crossing the `origin-rebase` / `origin-definition` cycle.

**After the swap** (`finishRebase`) the defined origin is **cleared**: the
project is now expressed in that frame, so the offset from it is zero, and the
grid goes back to the world axes. `state.triangulationResults` (and each
session's) is dropped rather than transformed — it is derived from 3D that just
moved — and the 3D viewport is rebuilt, not merely re-framed, because its
skeletons are built from `points3d`.

**Imports from project modules.** `./progress-delta.js` (`noteProgress`, the
lit stretch on the re-base bar), `./app-state.js` (`state`, `viewport3d`),
`../import-export/save-load.js` (`setStatus`, `markDirty`, `quickSave`),
`../import-export/file-io.js` (`exportCalibrationTOML`, `downloadTOML`),
`../loading/calibration-pick.js` (`REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME`),
`../pose/origin-rebase.js` (`countRebaseTargets`, `subsetRebaseTally`,
`planOriginRebase`, `applyOriginRebase`), `./rendering.js` (`drawAllOverlays`),
`../pose/initialization.js` (`update3DViewport`), and
**circularly** `./origin-definition.js` (`originState`, `renderOriginResult`) +
`./plane-definition.js` (`planeModel`, `syncPlanes3D`, `refreshPlanePanel`),
call-time use only.

**Imported by.** `ui/origin-definition.js` (which owns the button).

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/set-new-calibration.mjs` — the inventory, Cancel at the
confirmation and mid-flight (no file written, no coordinate moved), and the
commit to a stand-in `FileSystemFileHandle` (headless Chromium rejects
`showSaveFilePicker` with `AbortError`): every probe point keeps its pixel, the
origin collapses, the file name and both warnings.
`tests/e2e/origin-rebase-slp-diff.mjs` diffs every HDF5 dataset of `project.slp`
across the re-base, with a negative control for a leftover `calibration.toml`.

---

### ui/calibration-notice.js

**Purpose.** The modal that tells the user a multi-session project's sessions do
not all carry the same calibration. Raised at the END of a multi-session load,
once every session is in `state.sessions`.

**Why it has to exist at all: the failure is INVISIBLE.** Each session still
reprojects correctly against its own cameras, so every error stays small and
every number plausible; only the comparison BETWEEN sessions is broken, and
nothing on screen performs it.

**Silent in the ordinary case, and that is the point.** The modal opens only
when `compareSessionCalibrations` reports `ok === false`. A note on every
multi-session load would be worse than no note.

**It is a NOTE, not a prompt.** One dismiss button, and it changes nothing.
Fixing the divergence here would mean writing to the user's session folders
during a load, which is not what a load is for.

**It says WHO differs and nothing else.** One fixed title — *Sessions with
different calibrations detected* — then one `.calib-notice-group` section per
distinct calibration: a letter (`Calibration A`), `N sessions · M cameras` on
the title row, and the session FOLDER NAMES one per line, monospace, capped at
10 with `and N more`. Plus one line naming any session excluded for having no
calibration at all. That is the entire modal; the e2e test asserts the old
explanatory prose (lead paragraph, per-`kind` explanation, measured offset,
consequence, remedies) stays absent. Three rules follow:
- **No branch on `report.frameOnly`.** The title and the layout are the same for
  all four `kind`s, because the user's next move is the same: open the folder
  the modal names. `noteSessionCalibrationDivergence` LOGS the classification
  with the measured offset instead.
- **The largest group is listed FIRST** (`report.reference`, so the modal cannot
  disagree with the console line), which puts the odd session out last.
- **The letter is not read off the file name.** Two groups routinely both loaded
  a file called `calibration.toml`, so the letter is the only handle a group
  has.
The remedy lives where the divergence is created: the `Set as New Calibration`
confirmation's `#originRebaseMultiNote`.

**Key exports.** `noteSessionCalibrationDivergence(sessions)` — compares, logs
(including the offset and `kind` per divergent group), shows the modal if
needed, and returns the report (or null). Wrapped in a try/catch: a load must
never fail because of a check that only prints a note.
`showCalibrationNoticeModal(report)` builds the dialog. Esc closes it, and it
reuses `.plane-confirm-overlay` plus `.origin-rebase-block` / `-block-title` /
`-block-note` for the scrim, z-index, height cap and sticky action row. Its own
classes — `.calib-notice-group{,-title,-count}`, `.calib-notice-session{,s}` —
are in `styles.css` beside those. Its titles are plain `<div>`s and stay
un-foldable: `ui/origin-rebase.js`'s folding rules are scoped to
`summary.origin-rebase-block-title`.

**Imports from project modules.** `../pose/calibration-compare.js`.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js` (`handleLoadMultiSession`),
`import-export/slp-import.js` (the multi-session `.slp` path).

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/multi-session-calibration-notice.mjs`.

---

### ui/plane-angle.js

**Purpose.** The dialog and the commit for **Set Angle Between Two Planes** —
the UI half of `pose/plane-angle.js`. That module plans purely; this one asks,
previews and writes, the same split `planPlaneFit` / `applyPlaneFit` uses: the
geometry must be inspectable and refusable before anything is written.

**Why a modal rather than a mode.** Set Origin is a wizard because its inputs
are PICKED in the 3D view. This action's inputs are two planes and a number,
which a form states better — and plane FILL meshes carry no `userData`, so only
corners are raycastable and there is no "click a plane" gesture to reuse.

**What the commit does, in order, because the order is load-bearing.**
1. write the rotated 3D through `writePoints3dForPlane` — the one sanctioned
   publish path, which skips LOCKED nodes and routes PLANE-LOCKED ones through
   `PlaneModel.constrainPoint3dForNode`.
2. install the ROTATED fit (`rotatePlaneFit`) rather than re-fitting, so the
   normal's sign cannot flip.
3. reproject the moved corners into every 2D view the plane is placed on,
   exactly as `applyPlaneFit` does. Only corners that MOVED are rewritten — a
   hinge corner's 2D is the user's observation of a point that did not move —
   and the reprojection reads each node's ACTUAL position, not the plan's,
   because the constrain hook may have moved a plane-locked node off it.
4. clear the stored fit of any OTHER plane whose nodes moved.
5. **Hold the moved nodes PLANE-LOCKED.** This is what makes the angle durable:
   without it the next Triangulate re-solves those corners from 2D and throws
   the angle away. Plane-locked rather than Locked because the user asserted
   the plane's ORIENTATION, not where each corner sits on it: a held corner is
   still re-solved and projected back onto the planes it belongs to, so the
   annotation keeps improving while the angle survives. A node already Locked
   stays Locked. No plane is nominated (the pin takes none). It happens LAST
   because step 1 writes through the same constrain hook and until step 2 the
   stored fit is the OLD one — pinning earlier would project the rotation
   straight back onto the plane it was being rotated off.

   **A held corner shared with another plane may miss the target**, and that is
   what `plane_locked_elsewhere` warns about: the pin holds it in that plane
   too. Reported, never refused.

**Which planes it offers: usable, not fitted.** A plane loses its stored
`planeFit` for reasons that say nothing about whether it HAS a plane — pinning a
node clears the fit of every plane on it (this action's own auto-lock included),
and a triangulated but never-Fitted plane never had one — so `fittedPlanes()`,
what Set Origin asks, hid most of a user's planes. Set Origin needs the stored
fit; this action only needs to know WHERE the plane is. So `usableFit` returns
the stored fit or derives one via the model's `usablePlaneFit` (shared with the
`plane-locked` pin, so they cannot disagree), and **nothing is written**:
opening a dialog must not mutate the project. The PLAIN fit, not the
constrained one: it is rotated with the points, and a TLS fit is exactly
equivariant under a rigid motion where an anchor-constrained fit is not.

**Key exports.**
- `usableFit(plane, model)` → the plane's stored fit, else one derived now, else
  null (fewer than 3 solved corners, or they are coincident or collinear). A
  thin wrapper over `PlaneModel`'s `usablePlaneFit`.
- `anglePlanes()` → every plane with a usable fit, in model order.
- `showPlaneAngleModal()` — the dialog: two plane `<select>`s (picking the same
  plane in both swaps them rather than producing a dead form), a swap button, a
  readout of the current angle and the hinge, a target field, the plan's
  warnings (each line the warning's `message`, its optional `detail` — the
  untruncated list — as the line's `title`), Cancel / Apply. **Esc cancels**
  (capture-phase `keydown`). Refuses up front with `showPlaneDialog` when fewer
  than two planes are usable. The derived fits are handed to `planAngleEdit` as
  its `fits` argument (a test seam; see `pose/plane-angle.js`).
- `applyAngleEdit(plan, fixedPlane, movingPlane)` → `{ok, movedPx,
  skippedNames, constrainedNames, heldNames, stalePlaneNames, achievedDeg}`.
  One `markDirty()`, then the panel's mutation quartet.
- `renderAngleButton()` — enable/disable `#btnSetPlaneAngle` with the reason in
  its `title`, modelled on `renderOriginButton`. **Two** usable planes, not one:
  an angle is a relationship.
- `setupPlaneAngle()` — wire the button; called once from
  `setupPlaneDefinition`.
- `closeAngleModal()` — tear the dialog down, clear the ghost and the role
  outlines, and lift the edit lock. Exported for `exitPlaneMode`.
- `isAngleModalOpen()` — read by the three edit-lock guards below.

**It is the one plane dialog that is NOT modal.** Its inputs are two planes the
user has to ORBIT to tell apart, so:
- the backdrop draws no scrim and takes `pointer-events: none` (the dialog takes
  the pointer back), leaving the 3D view live underneath;
- `parkBesideViewport` opens it to the LEFT of `#viewport3dContainer` (falling
  back to the screen's left edge, never off it); the header still drags it;
- the two planes wear matching coloured outlines in the scene
  (`viewport3d.setPlaneRoles`), keyed to a swatch beside each dropdown —
  `ROLE_FIXED_COLOR` `#4da3ff` for the one held, `ROLE_MOVING_COLOR` `#ffd24d`
  for the one that moves. Not the planes' own colours, which would make the
  roles mean something different in every project.
- **Apply re-plans before committing**: the scene could have changed since the
  last input change, and the plan is pure, so re-running it is free.

Esc still cancels: that listener is on the document, not the backdrop.

**But "live view" is not "live data" — the EDIT LOCK.** Everything the dialog
says is read from plane geometry, so while it is open plane data is frozen in
all three places it can be edited from:
- `refreshPlanePanel` ends in `applyAngleModalLock`, which disables every
  `button` / `select` / `input` in `#planePanel` and puts `plane-angle-lock` on
  `<body>` (which dims the panel and stops `<summary>` elements folding);
- `syncPlanes3D` pushes `editable: false`, making 3D corners inert;
- `beginPlaneDrag` refuses a 2D corner drag with a status line saying why, and
  two siblings close the paths that do not go through it: the
  `isPlaneDataLocked` callback (the right-click null toggle) and a guard in
  `handlePlaneDrop` (a plane row dropped onto a view — a `<tr>` takes no
  `disabled`).

All three ask `isAngleModalOpen()` on every render rather than being toggled by
hand, so there is no lock state to get out of step; unlocking needs no
bookkeeping because those renders recompute each control's real disabled state.
What stays live: orbiting, zooming, panning, selection, the ghost preview, the
dialog's own Cancel / Apply (in `document.body`, not the locked panel), and pose
annotation. `exitPlaneMode` calls `closeAngleModal`: a dialog that holds this
panel locked must not outlive it.

**The live plan.** Every input change re-runs `planAngleEdit` and stores the
result; Apply commits that plan (after the re-plan above), so it can never act
on a staler state than the readout shows.

**The target field.** Commit-on-`change` with **revert-on-invalid** — and the
EMPTY case is checked before `Number`, which would read `''` as 0 ("make these
planes parallel"), a silent, plausible and wrong answer. `keydown` is
`stopPropagation`'d so the app's hotkeys do not fire while typing.

**DOM it owns.** `#planeAngleOverlay` (`.plane-confirm-overlay`, reused
wholesale), `.plane-angle-modal`, `#planeAngleFixed`, `#planeAngleMoving`,
`#btnPlaneAngleSwap`, `#planeAngleReadout`, `#planeAngleTarget`,
`#planeAngleWarn`, `#btnPlaneAngleCancel`, `#btnPlaneAngleApply`. The panel
button `#btnSetPlaneAngle` is static markup in `index.html`, in its own
`.panel-button-row` after `#btnSetOrigin`; it is caught automatically by origin
mode's `lockUI` selector (`.plane-panel button`) and so must **not** be added to
`UNLOCKED_IDS`. Styles: `styles.css` § "Set Angle Between Two Planes".

**No keyboard shortcut**, so no `ACTION_CATALOG` entry — matching Set Origin.

**Imports from project modules.** `./plane-definition.js` (circular, call-time
only — `planeModel`, `planeState`, `refreshPlanePanel`, `syncPlanes3D`,
`showPlaneDialog`, `refreshTriangulationErrors`), `../pose/plane-data.js`
(`writePoints3dForPlane`, `usablePlaneFit`), `../pose/triangulation.js`
(`reprojectPointCamera`), `../pose/plane-angle.js`,
`./app-state.js`, `../import-export/save-load.js`, `./rendering.js`.

**Imported by.** `ui/plane-definition.js` (`setupPlaneAngle`,
`renderAngleButton`, `isAngleModalOpen`, `closeAngleModal`).

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/plane-angle.mjs` — gating, the dialog, the ghost, Apply,
the auto-hold, a pin-cleared plane still offered (§8), parking beside the 3D
view (§9), the edit lock including closing restoring each control to its OWN
state (§10), and the Triangulate locked-nodes modal (§5b, its only coverage).
§4/§5 make the hold observable (a Triangulate against perturbed 2D keeps 90°
when held, walks off when Free) and measure the angle from the CORNERS
(`__pointAngle`), because a stored fit is not re-derived when a node moves.

### ui/mesh-objects.js

**Purpose.** The **3D Mesh Objects table** — a third table under Nodes and
Planes, for the third layer of the model (a node is a point, a plane is a group
of nodes, an object is a group of planes). Create, name, recolour and delete an
object; add and remove its member planes; and read a connectivity report of the
shape those planes imply. No orientation control — winding is derived (see
`pose/mesh-object-geometry.js`).

Deliberately its OWN module rather than more of `ui/plane-definition.js`: this
feature is strictly additive, and keeping it separate is what makes "nothing
existing changes" checkable — `plane-definition.js` gains one import, one
`setupMeshObjects()` call and one `refreshMeshObjectsPanel()` call.

**Key exports.** `meshObjectState` (`{selectedObjectId}` — panel-local, NOT
persisted), `getSelectedMeshObject()`, `meshObjectGeometry(obj)` (builds in the
applied origin frame at scale 1, so reported numbers match the viewport),
`refreshMeshObjectsPanel()`, `createMeshObject(name)`, `deleteObject(obj)`,
`setupMeshObjects()`.

**Everything shown is DERIVED**, recomputed from the model on every render: the
3D can move at any moment (a node drag, a re-triangulate, a fit, an origin
change), and `buildMeshObjectGeometry` is cheap at this scale.

**The report says only what the reader can act on.** A multi-shell object says
that joining requires SHARING a node, and coincident-but-unshared nodes are
named as pairs — the "I thought I joined it" case, invisible in the viewport
because the two corners draw on top of each other. It **says nothing about
normals** (winding is derived, with no user control), and a single-shell OPEN
object gets no line — open is the ordinary state for a cage with no lid. Closed
still reports its volume. Pinned by `tests/e2e/mesh-object-roundtrip.mjs` §2.

**There is no Shape column.** A per-row badge (`closed`, `2 shells`, …) used
terms the panel defined nowhere; what is actionable is in the selected object's
report. A row is swatch / name / planes / delete, and the table does not derive
every object's geometry on every repaint. `tests/e2e/mesh-object-roundtrip.mjs`
§2 asserts the badge, the header cell and the fifth `<td>` are gone.

**Membership is edited by PICK-AND-ADD**, the Planes section's idiom:
`renderMembers` lists the planes IN the object, each with an × to take it out,
and `renderAddPlaneSelect` offers a `<select>` of the NON-members plus `+ Add`
(`#meshObjectAddPlaneSelect` / `#btnAddMeshObjectPlane`). An empty picker —
every plane already a member — is said in words with the button disabled. A
member whose plane was since deleted still gets a (greyed) row, because its × is
the only way to clear the stale id.

**What this module may NOT do.** Nothing here creates, deletes or edits a node or
a plane. An object is a grouping: dissolving it cannot destroy its members, and
removing a plane from an object must not delete the plane. The panel's only
destructive action is the object's own ×, which removes the grouping and nothing
else — which is what keeps 3D Mesh Objects from breaking a workflow that
predates them.

**Export.** `Export .stl` / `Export .glb` (`#btnExportMeshStl` /
`#btnExportMeshGlb`) sit in the editor, because they act on the SELECTED
object. Both write the geometry `meshObjectGeometry` reports on — origin frame
applied, scale 1 — so the file and the report cannot describe different shapes.
They are DISABLED, with the reason in the tooltip, until the object has
triangulated faces (both writers would refuse). Neither marks the project dirty:
an export writes a file, not a change. The formats and the one sanctioned axis
conversion are `import-export/mesh-export.js`.

**Imports from project modules.** `./plane-definition.js` — `planeModel`,
`refreshPlanePanel`, `syncPlanes3D` (circular, and safe for the same reason the
rest of this feature's cycles are: every use is inside a function body);
`./origin-definition.js` — `originState`; `../pose/mesh-object-geometry.js`;
`../import-export/save-load.js` — `setStatus`, `markDirty`;
`../import-export/mesh-export.js` — `meshObjectToSTL`, `meshObjectToGLB`,
`isExportable`, `meshFilenameStem`; `../import-export/file-io.js` —
`downloadBytes`.

**Imported by.** `ui/plane-definition.js`.

**Selection is visible.** The selected object's row carries `.plane-selected`,
styled by `#meshObjectsTable tbody tr.plane-selected` exactly as the Planes
table styles its own selection.

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/mesh-object-roundtrip.mjs` (the panel through real DOM
handlers, the membership editor, the dirty flag per mutation, save → reopen by
ID, the PROJECT scope, and three negative controls: untouched projects write no
key, the feature is additive, and deleting an object keeps its planes and
nodes); `tests/e2e/mesh-membership-highlight.mjs` (what the 3D viewport draws
for a selection); `tests/e2e/mesh-object-export.mjs` (the two export buttons).

---

### ui/seekbar-markers.js

**Purpose.** Possible-ID-switch ticks on the transport seekbar (`#seekbarMarks`
inside `#seekbar`), for the active session's ID Switches results.

**Key exports.** `installSeekbarMarkers(layerEl, totalFrames)`;
`setSeekbarSwitchMarkers(markers, totalFrames)` (`[]` clears) /
`getSeekbarSwitchMarkers()`; `setSeekbarMarkerFrames(totalFrames)` (re-lays the
ticks out only when the frame count changed — called from `updateSeekbarVisual`);
`seekbarMarkerAt(frac, widthPx, tolPx = 5)` (the tick nearest the cursor; a change
point beats a repeat); `describeSwitchMarker(m)` (the tooltip text).

**Drawing.** One tick per marker at `frame / (totalFrames - 1)` — the thumb's own
mapping: amber = size, cyan = images, violet = coat brightness, grey = the
single-camera review list (its tooltip: "place to review: id_0 ↔ id_1", no score); a "Both" tick wears
its two checks' colours (`cue-both-<earlier>-<later>`; drawn once, on the earlier
check's marker — size, then images, then brightness — its twin skipped), follow-on fainter and shorter,
still-swapped repeat a short faint tick, `reviewed` (ticked in the tab) dimmed.
Over the track, under the thumb, `pointer-events: none`: the seekbar's
mousedown/drag handlers (`ui/ui-wiring.js`) snap to a tick within 5 px, and the
seekbar tooltip names it ("Frame 18,105 · 05:01.733 — possible ID switch: id_1 ↔
id_2 (size score -1415)").

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/id-switch-modal.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/size-switch-check.mjs` (ticks drawn, none on the
timeline, hover text, click snaps to the tick's frame, reviewed dimming).

---

### ui/seekbar-ticks.js

**Purpose.** A time ruler under the transport seekbar (`#seekbarRuler` inside
`#seekbar`): labelled major ticks (`00:00  05:00  10:00 …`) with unlabelled
minor ticks between them, so a moment can be found and clicked by eye without
hovering for the tooltip.

**Key exports.** `seekbarTimeTicks(totalFrames, fps, widthPx, {charPx}?)` ->
`{step, minorStep, majors: [{frac, seconds, label, end}], minors: [frac]}`
(pure; the last major is the end tick); `formatTickLabel(seconds, decimals)`
(`mm:ss`, `h:mm:ss` from an hour up, rounded before splitting);
`formatDurationLabel(seconds, decimals)` (the end tick's label: rounded to the
millisecond like `formatTimestamp`, then TRUNCATED, so it is a prefix of the
readout's total); `TICK_STEPS` and the spacing constants
(`MIN_LABEL_SPACING_PX`, `LABEL_SPACING_WIDTHS`, `MIN_MINOR_SPACING_PX`,
`MAX_MINOR_PER_MAJOR`, `DEFAULT_CHAR_PX`); the DOM half,
`installSeekbarTicks(layerEl, {getTotalFrames, getFps})` and
`refreshSeekbarTicks()`.

**Behaviour.**
- **The step follows the video and the bar.** The major step is the SMALLEST of
  `TICK_STEPS` (1/2/5 below a minute, 1/2/5/10/15/30 min, then hours) whose
  labels are at least `max(60 px, 1.75 label widths)` apart at the bar's
  width. The minor step is the smallest nice step that divides the major into at
  most 6 parts and is still 8 px apart. Neither is shorter than one frame. No
  ruler when `00:00` and the duration do not both fit, or without a frame rate
  (fps 0 while a session has no video).
- **The bar's end is always marked**: a tick at the last frame, labelled with
  the duration — the readout's total, `totalFrames / fps`, truncated to the
  ruler's precision (`14:23` for `14:23.617`, never `14:24`), in bold
  (`.is-end`). It wins every collision: a regular label too close to it loses
  its text (the tick stays), and a regular or minor tick within 8 px of it is
  dropped, since it would read as a double tick.
- **Same mapping and same clock as the rest of the bar.** A tick for time `t`
  sits at `t·fps / (totalFrames − 1)` — the thumb's and the ID-switch ticks'
  mapping at the readout's frame-start time — so a click on `05:00` lands on the
  frame the readout shows as `05:00.000`. Labels are the readout's format
  without the milliseconds; a decimal only for sub-second steps.
- **Part of the seekbar, not a strip beside it.** The layer is a child of
  `#seekbar`, so a click or drag on a label scrubs and hovering one shows the
  seekbar tooltip. It hangs below the 24 px bar box; `.seekbar-container`'s
  `margin-bottom` reserves the room. Painted under the ID-switch marks and the
  thumb. Every label is centred on its tick, the two ends included, so `00:00`
  and the duration hang half a label past the bar's ends into
  `.seekbar-container`'s side margins (12 px, 16 px at <= 640 px), clear of the
  transport buttons and the FPS pill.
- **Never re-rendered per frame.** `refreshSeekbarTicks` (called from
  `updateSeekbarVisual` on every frame, and from `refreshReadoutTotals` /
  `clearReadout`) returns unless the frame count, FPS or width changed; a
  `ResizeObserver` on the layer re-lays it when the bar's width changes.
  Positions are percentages; the label font's character width is measured once
  (a hidden probe).

**Imports from project modules.** None (dependency-free, so the tick choice runs
in Node).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (installed in the seekbar-scrubbing IIFE in
`setupUI`; refreshed by `updateSeekbarVisual`), `ui/frame-readout.js`
(`refreshSeekbarTicks`).

**Coverage.** `tests/test-seekbar-ticks.mjs` (label format and the step choice
over a grid of frame rates × durations × widths, including the end tick and
the duration label being a prefix of `formatTimestamp`) and
`tests/e2e/seekbar-ticks.mjs` (real app: placement, no overlap, end labels clear
of the controls, clicks and hover, re-layout on resize / FPS change, zero ruler
DOM mutations over 200 frame steps, fits at 640 px).

---

### ui/seekbar-preview.js

**Purpose.** A thumbnail of the SELECTED view at the hovered frame, above the
seekbar's hover tooltip — so you can see what is at a moment before you click
there.

**Key exports.** `installSeekbarPreview(seekbar, {getView, devicePixelRatio?})`
-> `{show(frame, x, barWidth), hide(), element}` (handed to
`installSeekbarTooltip` as its `preview` option; `getView()` -> `{name, decoder,
videoWidth, videoHeight}` | null); `previewLeft(x, barWidth, boxWidth)` (the
box's centre: over the cursor, clamped over the bar, centred when the bar is
narrower); `createThumbCache(max)` (an LRU of thumbnails that closes evicted
bitmaps); `PREVIEW_WIDTH` (224 CSS px — `.seekbar-preview-canvas` in
`styles.css` must match), `THUMB_CACHE` (48). DOM: `#seekbarPreview`
(`.seekbar-preview` > canvas + `.seekbar-preview-label`).

**Behaviour.**
- **Which view.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`'s `getView`: the solo view in single-view
  mode, else the active dock pane's (`getActiveView`, the `.video-selected`
  cell), else the first. NOT `interactionManager.lastInteractedView` — mousemove
  over a view sets that, so it would follow the pointer across whichever views
  it crossed on its way down to the bar.
- **Frames come from `decoder.peekFrame(frame, canvasWidth)`**
  (`loading/video.js`), which never moves playback, the step cursor or the frame
  cache; hovering therefore cannot disturb the views or a paused step. A view
  whose decoder is missing or has no `peekFrame` gets no box (the tooltip
  alone).
- **One decode at a time, the newest wins.** A decode can be a keyframe run, far
  longer than a mousemove, so frames hovered meanwhile are not queued: when it
  lands, the latest hovered frame is next and the rest are skipped. Each result
  is drawn as it lands, dimmed (`.is-pending`) while it is not the frame under
  the cursor. Another view's image is cleared, never shown under this one's
  label. A null result (e.g. an embedded-frames `.slp`, which holds only the
  labelled frames) shows "No image at this frame" (`.is-empty`); nulls are not
  cached, since one can only mean the video was still loading.
- The last `THUMB_CACHE` thumbnails stay (keyed by decoder + its source, since
  a pooled decoder `switchSource`s and stays the same object), so going back
  over a stretch already hovered is instant.
- **Layout-free while moving**, like the tooltip: the box has a fixed width, so
  it is clamped without measuring; the canvas is resized only when the view's
  aspect ratio changes. Stacked above the bubble by CSS (`bottom: calc(100% +
  32px)`), `pointer-events: none`.

**Imports from project modules.** None (dependency-free, like the tooltip; the
pure half runs in Node).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (inside the seekbar-scrubbing IIFE in
`setupUI`).

**Coverage.** `tests/test-seekbar-preview.mjs` (`previewLeft`, the LRU) and
`tests/e2e/seekbar-preview.mjs` (real app: the active pane's view at the
hovered frame, above the bubble and clamped; follows the active pane and the
solo view but not `lastInteractedView`; never calls `getFrame` or moves the
frame; newest-wins coalescing with a slow decoder and cache hits; hidden while
dragging / on leave; "No image"; and `peekFrame` itself on
`tests/fixtures/barcode-60` — frame-accurate on mediabunny and HTML5, leaving
the step cursor, the frame cache and the playback `<video>` untouched).

---

### ui/seekbar-tooltip.js

**Purpose.** Hover tooltip on the transport seekbar (#142): "Frame 1,234 ·
00:41.100" for the point under the cursor, so you can see where a click or
drag will land before making it — and a hover line on the bar marking that
frame.

**Key exports.** `formatTimestamp(seconds)` (`mm:ss.mmm`, `h:mm:ss.mmm` from an
hour up; rounds to the millisecond before splitting, so never `00:60.000`),
`seekbarTooltipText(frameIdx, fps)`, `installSeekbarTooltip(seekbar,
{frameAtFraction, getTotalFrames, getFps, markerAt?, hoverLine?, preview?})` -> the
tooltip element (`markerAt(frac, widthPx)` -> `{frame, text}` | null: a
possible-ID-switch tick under the cursor — its frame replaces
`frameAtFraction`'s, as the scrub handlers snap to it, and its text is
appended; `hoverLine`: the `#seekbarHoverLine` element, from `index.html`;
`preview`: `{show(frame, x, barWidth), hide()}`, the controller
`ui/seekbar-preview.js` returns — shown with the tooltip at the same frame,
hidden during a drag like the hover line)
(`#seekbarTooltip`, `.seekbar-tooltip`).

**Behaviour.**
- The frame shown is EXACTLY the one a click there seeks to: the caller passes
  the scrub handlers' own `frameAtFraction` mapping. It is shown **1-based**, like
  `#currentFrame` and every other frame number on screen.
- The timestamp is the frame's start time, `frameIdx / state.fps`, and is left
  out when the frame rate is unknown (`state.fps` is 0 while a session has no
  video yet).
- Visible while hovering the bar and throughout a scrub drag (document-level
  mousemove); hidden on leave / release outside, and never shown when
  `totalFrames <= 1`. Centred on the cursor and clamped to the bar's extent;
  `pointer-events: none` so it cannot steal the hover.
- **The hover line** (`#seekbarHoverLine`, `.seekbar-hover-line`) marks the same
  frame ON the bar, at that frame's own position (`frame / (totalFrames − 1)`)
  rather than at the raw cursor — so it snaps onto an ID-switch tick exactly as
  a click does, and is never clamped like the bubble. It wears the ID Switches
  tab's playhead colours (`.id-switch-phead`) but is bigger than an ID-switch
  tick (4 x 24 px vs 3 x 16 px) so it reads among them. Its place in
  `index.html`'s markup, at the ticks' z-index, paints it over the ticks and
  under the thumb. Shown and hidden with the tooltip, except during a drag
  (the thumb already sits on the frame). A release over the bar re-runs the
  hover for the release point, which brings the line (and the preview) back.
- **Layout-free while moving**, so hovering during playback adds no forced
  synchronous layout: the bar's rect is cached on mouseenter / drag start
  (dropped by a `ResizeObserver` and window `resize`), and the bubble is
  measured only when its text LENGTH changes (monospace font).

**Imports from project modules.** None (dependency-free, so the text half runs
in Node).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (installed inside the seekbar-scrubbing
IIFE in `setupUI`), `ui/frame-readout.js` (`formatTimestamp`, so the frame
readout's time row reads exactly as this tooltip does).

**Coverage.** `tests/test-seekbar-tooltip.mjs` (formatting) and
`tests/e2e/seekbar-tooltip.mjs` (real mouse events: tooltip frame == the frame a
click lands on, drags, clamping, no timestamp at fps 0, zero layout reads across
hover moves; the hover line's position, colours, size, z-order and tick
snapping).

---

### ui/sessions-panes.js

**Purpose.** Dockview pane manager (video panes), the view strip, the
sessions panel, the session strip, the move-video modal, session
add/remove/switch, and view-strip thumbnails. Owns the on-screen
multi-video docking layout.

**Key exports.**
- `panelRenderers` — Map of panelId → VideoPaneRenderer. Each pane carries
  the zoom badge (`.zoom-badge.tri-dropdown`, shown while zoomed or at native
  resolution): `.unzoom-btn` shows the zoom as a percentage of 1:1
  (`zoomPercentLabel`) and a click fits; hover opens `.zoom-preset-menu` with
  `ZOOM_PRESET_PERCENTS` and Fit for THAT pane's view (`setZoomPercent` /
  `resetZoom`; items carry `data-zoom-pct` / `data-zoom-fit`). 100% is ticked
  only when the view is AT 1:1 (`zoom.native`). The badge stops `mousedown` so a
  click on it never starts the cell's pan / box zoom.
- `multiSelectViews`, `clearMultiSelect`.
- `refreshPaneInteractions()` — after any pane rebuild: re-attach the
  interaction manager, give a view a zoom state the FIRST time it is docked
  (`initZoom` only when `view.zoom` is missing), re-wire the zoom handlers when
  its cell changed, fit the canvases, then `reapplyZoom` every docked view
  (pan rescaled, a 1:1 scale re-derived for the new box), and seek + redraw.
  **It must not re-init an existing zoom**, or every view snaps back to fit
  after a grid ↔ solo switch. `reapplyZoom` runs here, not only in the
  ResizeObserver, so the new cell's FIRST paint is zoomed.
  **The one exception is View ▸ Native Resolution:** while it is on, a view
  coming on screen joins it at 1:1 (its `zoom.native` set before the
  `reapplyZoom`). The mode is read as it stood BEFORE the rebuild, then
  re-derived for the views now on screen (`nativeResolutionActive()`), since it
  is otherwise refreshed only by a zoom or by opening the View menu. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/zoom-survives-pane-rebuild.mjs` and
  `tests/e2e/native-resolution-zoom.mjs` §12.
- `scrollViewStripTo(viewName)` — scroll that view's strip item into sight
  (`.view-strip-list` scrolls), so arrow-cycling the solo view never moves the
  highlight off screen. Called by `cycleSingleView`.
- `activatePanelForView(viewName)` — activate the first docked panel showing a
  view, returning false when it isn't docked. That `setActive()` drives the
  yellow `strip-selected` highlight, `lastInteractedView` and the 3D camera
  highlight (see `onDidActivePanelChange`), so it is the single way to say
  "select this view". Shared by `addVideoPanel`'s duplicate guard, both view
  strip handlers, and `ui/ui-wiring.js`'s `setGridMode`.
- `clampRotation` (a **re-export** of `ui/video-filters.js`'s, which is where the
  function now lives — existing importers are unaffected), `syncRotationUI`.
- `applyVideoFilters(view)` — write the COMBINED brightness+contrast CSS filter
  onto a view's canvas (see below).
- `restoreViewRotation(view)` — re-seed `view.rotation` from the session, the
  non-CSS counterpart of `applyVideoFilters` (see below).
- `populateViewStrip`, `populateSessionsPanel`, `populateSessionStrip`.
- `showMoveVideoModal`.
- `removeSession`, `switchSession` (async).

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./id-switch-modal.js` — `refreshIdSwitchPanel`: `switchSession` shows the new
  session's ID-switch results and markers (try/catch, like `populateTimelineVisibility`).
- `./app-state.js` — `state`, controllers + setters.
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `FrameGroup`, `UnlinkedInstance`, `Camera`,
  `someValidPoint3d`, `pooledPoints3d` (the re-solved groups' 3D).
- `../pose/lazy-residency.js` — `hydrateFrameMembers2d`, `releaseFrameMembers2d`,
  `dropGroupDerivedReprojections`. **The move keeps only the re-solved 3D**: each
  group losing the view drops its reprojections, reprojected instances and its
  entry in the frame's results, and the display fill (`ui/rendering.js`)
  re-derives them when the frame is shown, keeping the move's derived state
  bounded (`tests/e2e/load-export-move-bounded-derived-state.mjs` (M)).
  `moveVideosToSession` re-solves every origin-session group that loses the
  moved view; on a lazy project a member of a non-resident frame is a `_lazy2d`
  placeholder, which triangulates to nothing and would leave the group's old
  points3d (solved WITH the moved view). So each affected frame's members are
  hydrated for the re-solve and released after
  (`tests/e2e/move-video-lazy-members.mjs`). Still resident-only: step 1 moves
  the moved camera's 2D for RESIDENT frames only — its rows for every other
  frame stay in the origin session's lazy store.
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `triangulateAndReproject`,
  `getInstanceGroupsForFrame`,
  `sessionHasCalibration`, `resolveTriangulationMethod`. The groups a move
  strips a camera from are re-solved with `resolveTriangulationMethod(group)`
  and the result's method stamped back, so a bundle-adjusted group is not
  silently downgraded to DLT.
- `../loading/session-loader.js` — `cellResizeObserver`,
  `createViewForVideoFile`, `rebuildVideoController`,
  `fitCanvasesToCells`, `updateTotalFrames`.
- `../loading/video.js` — `OnDemandVideoDecoder`, `ZOOM_PRESET_PERCENTS`,
  `zoomRatioLabel` (the zoom badge's preset menu).
- `../import-export/save-load.js` — `setStatus`, `showLoading`,
  `hideLoading`, `quickSave`, `markDirty`.
- `./video-filters.js` — the `CONTRAST_*` / `BRIGHTNESS_*` / `ROTATION_*`
  bounds, `clampContrast` / `clampBrightness` / `clampRotation` /
  `clampRotationSetting`, `buildVideoFilter`, and the three per-session
  get/set pairs. `clampRotation` is re-exported (see Key exports).
- `./rendering.js` — `drawAllOverlays`, `setReprojErrorVisible`.
- `./ui-wiring.js` — `setSoloView`. A **cycle** (`ui-wiring.js` imports this
  module), hoist-safe because the only read is inside the view strip's click
  handler, which cannot run during module evaluation.
- `./info-panel.js` — `updateInfoPanel`, `populateTimelineVisibility`.
- `./loading-progress-modal.js` — `getLoadingProgressModal` (`switchSession`'s
  "Loading videos" progress).
- `./frame-readout.js` — `clearReadout` (session removed),
  `refreshReadoutTotals` (`switchSession` restoring a cached frame count / fps).
- `../pose/initialization.js` — `setup3DViewport`.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`,
`ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/view-align-modal.js`
(`syncRotationUI`), `loading/session-loader.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`.

**Decoder pool cold reserve.** `switchSession` keeps `state._decoderPoolCold[]`
beside `state.decoderPool[]`: when the incoming session has fewer cameras,
surplus decoders move to the cold reserve with a 60-second eviction timer, and
the next switch reuses them (cancelling the timer) before constructing new
`OnDemandVideoDecoder`s. This caps the pool at the current session's camera
count without destroying recently-used decoders immediately. A decoder going
into the reserve gets `releaseFrames()` at once: the reserve keeps the decoder,
not its decoded frames (1.8 GB of them right after a switch between 3 open
8-camera sessions, held for the 60 s). Test: `tests/test-decoder-pool-repeated-swap.js`.

**Per-session timeline height.** `switchSession` saves the timeline height on
the **outgoing** session (`oldSession._timelineHeight`,
`oldSession._timelineCollapsed`) and restores it on the **incoming** one;
first-visit sessions get `Math.min(timeline.getPreferredHeight(), 0.3 *
window.innerHeight)`. The save/restore is **inlined** via
`document.getElementById` rather than importing `timeline-controller.js`, so the
brace-walked `switchSession` test harnesses
(`test-session-switch-frame-reset.js`,
`test-switchsession-parallel-decoders.js`) need no extra stub. The same
constraint shapes the inlined `_uploadedCameras` recompute.

**Active-session memory model — dirty prompt + eviction.** `switchSession`
prompts Save/Discard/Cancel when leaving a dirty session (`_leaving.isDirty`,
per-session via `markDirty`/`clearDirty`); Save proceeds only if `quickSave()`
cleared the flag. It then frees the outgoing session's lazy state: if
`_leaving.lazyLoader` is set, it is closed and
`frameGroups`/`instanceGroups`/`triangulationResults` reset. On a multi-session
project a successful save (`saveAllSessionsStreaming`) has usually evicted every
`lazyLoader` already; this is what reclaims the memory for a single-lazy-session
project or the Discard path. Re-activating an evicted session requires reopening
it from scratch — there is no lazy re-hydration within one open project `.slp`.

**User-facing features.** Video pane docking (drag/move/resize), view
strip (top), session strip (bottom), per-pane brightness/contrast/rotation
controls, switch-session UX, move-video-between-sessions modal.

**View strip click behaviour.** Ctrl/Cmd+click multi-selects (for drag-docking
several views at once). A plain click clears the multi-selection and then does
exactly ONE of three things, in order:

1. in **single-view mode**, `setSoloView(name)` (`ui/ui-wiring.js`) makes that
   view the solo'd one, *replacing* the current pane, so solo mode stays solo;
2. if the view is already on screen, `activatePanelForView(name)` focuses its
   pane;
3. otherwise the view was **closed** (the pane's X) and `addVideoPanel(name)`
   **re-opens** it — the discoverable way back to a closed view.

**There is deliberately no `dblclick` handler.** A double-click is two clicks
landing on the same state, and a separate path would drift from the click one.
`addVideoPanel` refuses to dock a view twice (it activates the existing pane),
so duplicates come only from drag/drop docking and `addAllViewsAsGrid`. See
**Single-view ("solo") mode** under `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**`removeVideoPanel(viewName)` — the counterpart to `addVideoPanel`.** Closes
EVERY pane showing that view and returns how many it closed. Used by
`removeVideoFile` (`loading/session-loader.js`) so the Videos tab's **Remove
Video** takes the whole panel out of the dock. Three things about it:
- It goes through `panel.api.close()`, as the pane's own × and `clearAll` do —
  `onDidRemovePanel` is what decrements `dockedViews` and clears the strip's
  in-dock dot.
- **Every** pane, not the first: a view can be docked more than once, and a
  survivor would go on rendering a view that no longer exists.
- Panes are matched through `panelRenderers`, never by parsing the panel id —
  the id is `video-<name>-<counter>` and a view name may itself contain dashes.
It also deletes the `dockedViews` entry outright afterwards, so stale
bookkeeping cannot block a later re-add.

**`syncDockedViews()` — `fromJSON` builds panels behind `addVideoPanel`'s back.**
`paneManager.dockedViews` (viewName → pane count) is maintained by
`addVideoPanel` / `addAllViewsAsGrid` / `onDidRemovePanel`, and drives both the
strip's in-dock dot and `activatePanelForView`'s early-out. `api.fromJSON()` —
used by `setGridMode` to restore the cached grid layout — constructs panels
directly, leaving the counts EMPTY. `syncDockedViews()` re-derives them (and the
dots) by walking `api.panels` through `panelRenderers`; `setGridMode` calls it
immediately after `fromJSON`.

**`addAllViewsAsGrid()` leaves solo mode.** It sets `state.viewMode = 'grid'`
and removes the solo chip (`#viewModeIndicator` + the dock's
`has-view-indicator`). The loaders, session switches and `removeSession` all
rebuild the dock with `clearAll()` + `addAllViewsAsGrid()`, so without this the
grid would show with the mode still `'single'` and `v` would silently do
nothing. Pinned by `tests/e2e/solo-view-navigation.mjs` §6.

**Video display settings — brightness, contrast (issue #149) and rotation.**
`populateVideoBrightnessTable`, `populateVideoContrastTable` and
`populateVideoRotationTable` render the Visibility tab's per-view tables
(`#visVideoBrightnessTable`, `#visVideoContrastTable` — each with a `Select All
Videos` link toggle — and `#visVideoRotationTable`, which is per-camera only).

All three are stored **per SESSION** (`session.videoBrightness` /
`videoContrast` / `videoRotation`) and persisted in the `.slp` via
`import-export/visibility-metadata.js`. `switchSession` throws `state.views`
