away and rebuilds every pane, so a per-view field would silently reset. Any slider edit
calls `markDirty()`: these are project state, not browser-local preferences.

They restore in two different ways, because only two of them are CSS filters:
- **Brightness + contrast** funnel into the single
  **`applyVideoFilters(view)`**, which composes
  `buildVideoFilter(getSessionBrightness(...), getSessionContrast(...))` into ONE
  `style.filter` string. They must share one writer: both target the same CSS
  property, so applying them independently would have the second write erase the
  first.
- **Rotation** is not display-only — the renderer and hit-testing read
  `view.rotation`. **`restoreViewRotation(view)`** re-seeds that field from the
  session; without it a saved rotation would show in the table while the video
  rendered un-rotated.

`VideoPaneRenderer.init`/`update` call `restoreViewRotation` then
`applyVideoFilters` right after assigning `view.canvas`, which restores each
session's own settings on a switch or reopen. The hold-to-rotate
gesture in `ui/ui-wiring.js` keeps a FRACTIONAL `view.rotation` while animating
and commits the rounded degree to the session once, on keyup.
`applyVideoFilters` also writes the filter onto any **duplicate panes** of the
same view: they receive pixels via `drawImage` (`renderDuplicatePanels`), which
does not inherit the primary canvas's CSS filter.

**Visibility-tab toggle list refresh.** After `timeline.setData(newSession)`,
`switchSession` calls `populateTimelineVisibility(newSession)` (through the
existing `info-panel.js` import, preserving the brace-walked test contract) to
re-render the Views / Tracks / Identities toggle lists. The hidden sets
(`_hiddenCameras` / `_hiddenTracks` / `_hiddenIdentities`) live on each
`session`, so no explicit save/restore is needed. The call is wrapped in a `try`
so the headless test runner doesn't crash on a missing `document`.


---

### ui/id-switch-modal.js

**Purpose.** Tracks ▸ **Check ID Switches (Body Size)…** / **(Images)…**, and the
same checks run automatically after Track All / Track Frame Range. Runs
`checkSizeSwitches` / `checkImageSwitches` (`pose/id-switch-check.js`), puts every
change point and repeat on the transport seekbar (`setSeekbarSwitchMarkers`, `ui/seekbar-markers.js`; tagged
`cue: 'size' | 'image'`), and lists the change points in the right panel's
**ID Switches** tab as a per-session review checklist.

**Key exports.** `runIdSwitchChecks({size?, image?, auto?, statusPrefix?,
navigateToFrame?, inject?})`; `setIdSwitchNavigator(fn)` (ui-wiring registers
`navigateToFrame` once, so rows stay clickable when the tracker started the
check); `setIdSwitchRefresher(fn)` (ui-wiring registers the repaint run after a
fix / undo — overlays, 3D, info panel, timeline — keeping this module a leaf);
`setTrackSummaryOpener(fn)` (ui/track-summary-modal.js registers `reopenTrackSummary`
for the tab's **Show Track All summary** button — first in the tab, in both the
results and the empty state, shown only when the session has a summary from a
Track All in this page, since it is not saved);
`clickCheckFromSummary(menuId)` (the summary's **Check by images…** / **Check by
body size** click the menu item through this: the check runs exactly as from the
menu, but a one-shot flag — read and cleared by `runIdSwitchChecks` before its first
await, so only the check that click starts sees it — makes it end by reopening the
summary with its results (`reopenTrackSummary(session, results)`) instead of opening
the tab; with no summary kept for the session it opens the tab as from the menu);
`refreshIdSwitchPanel(session?)` (render the tab and put that session's
markers on the seekbar — called after a check, from `updateInfoPanel` and from
`switchSession`); `openIdSwitchPanel()` (show the panel, if hidden, on the tab);
`clearIdSwitchResults(session?)` (called by `runTrackingPass` before it relabels);
`setReviewList(session, result, navigateToFrame?)` (single-camera Track All puts its
review list on the session as `results.review` — see below);
`ID_SWITCH_LEAD_IN_SECONDS` (1), `ID_SWITCH_SINGLE_CAMERA_LEAD_SECONDS` (2),
`idSwitchLeadSeconds(session)` (2 on a single-camera session, else 1),
`idSwitchLeadInFrame(marker, fps, seconds?)`,
`updateIdSwitchProgress(frame)`; back-compat
`runSizeSwitchCheck`. `inject: {createEmbedder, createBrightnessSampler}` replaces
the image model and the coat-brightness sampler — test-only
(`tests/e2e/id-switch-image-check.mjs`, `tests/e2e/single-camera-track-all.mjs`).

**The review list (single camera).** After single-camera Track All the tab also
lists `results.review` (`pose/single-camera-tracking.js` `reviewResult`): the places
a switch is most likely — near-ties, input-tracklet changes, handovers. They are
rows like the checks' (ticks, seekbar markers, progress bar, Next unreviewed,
the saved checklist), drawn grey, with NO score; their note says why the place is
listed ("a lone animal's track changes", "an animal comes back after being out of sight", …); the header counts them ("N places to
look at") and "About these flags" explains the list first. A check row at the same
place and pair is ONE row, the check's, marked "on the review list" ('review' is
last in `ID_SWITCH_CUES`). **Fix on a review row** swaps from the frame you are on
(anywhere from the row's lead-in up to just before the NEXT place on the list) or
just after the place, to the END of the video — later rows fix their own
stretches. A fixed review row keeps a **Fix again…** button and lists every fix
made from it ("Fixed: … on frames a–end; again on frames b–end"): labels that swap
back before the next place (a short flip, seen while playing the row through)
take a second fix from where they do. **Every row that stands for a place** has
that fix too (`idSwitchPlaceOf`: a review-list place, or a check's row at a moment
such as an animal coming back after being out of sight — `look`, so on one camera
every coat-brightness row at a candidate moment or a return). Unfixed, a check's
row shows the place's fix as **Fix from here…** beside its own **Fix switch…**;
once fixed, as **Fix again…** (both carry `data-place`, and the dialog then
explains the place's fix), and the row stays selected. Before 2026-10-29 only a
review-list row offered it before the check's Fix, and a fixed check row with a
return behind it offered only Undo: the walkthrough's reviewer could not make 13
place fixes on the 64 hard SLAP-2M views (0.38 points) and 4 on sham_gdx; with it,
none (SLAP 98.61% -> 98.91%, sham_gdx 99.95% -> 99.97%). A check's fix starting a
second too early once cost a sham_gdx video 90.2% instead of 99.9%. With more than two tracks it lets the reviewer
pick the two tracks (`pairSelect`, `#idSwitchFixA` / `#idSwitchFixB`, defaulting to
the row's pair): on 3–4 mice the row's own pair was the swapped one in only a third
to two thirds of the switches found. The dialog keeps Esc / Cancel.

**The coat-brightness check** (`brightness: true`, `runBrightness`) runs
`checkBrightnessSwitches` with `ui/brightness-sampler.js`'s vectors under the same
cancellable progress dialog as images ("Reading coat brightness in N views: frame
i of n"), reading `brightnessCheckHz` (4) and `brightnessCheckThreshold` (-800).
It needs the videos but no model; without videos it says so. Its rows and ticks
are violet; "About these flags" names it and its sampling.

**Runs automatically after tracking.** `pose/tracker.js`'s `runTrackingPass` calls
`runIdSwitchChecks({auto: true, statusPrefix, size, image})` after BOTH Track All
and Track Frame Range: size when the Tracking Wizard's `autoSwitchCheck` is on
(default), images when `autoImageSwitchCheck` is on (default OFF — minutes, needs
the videos; with no GPU it runs on the CPU rather than being skipped). Auto mode
appends each check's result to the pass's status line, opens the ID Switches tab
only when a possible switch is found, and reports a check that cannot run as
"skipped — reason", never as a failure of the pass. It always analyses the WHOLE
session's identities. Each result carries `elapsedMs` (wall-clock, model
download and decoding included), shown by the Track All summary box
(`ui/track-summary.js`); it is not saved — `serializeIdSwitchReview`
(`ui/id-switch-review.js`) picks its fields explicitly.
After a SINGLE-CAMERA Track All the coat-brightness check runs instead of body
size (`autoBrightnessSwitchCheck`, default on), and images when
`autoImageSwitchCheck` is on; size is optional there (menu / summary box).

**On a single-camera session** (`pose/single-camera-tracking.js`) the checks read
`singleCameraCheckSession`'s stand-in (the view's 2D as `points3d` (x, y, 0)), and
the coat-brightness check also tests Track All's candidate moments
(`session._idSwitchCandidates`) with `skipEmpty` / `clearestEndLeads`. A moment's
row reads "at …" instead of "close …" and says why it was looked at. A Fix swaps
the two TRACKS (`swapSingleCameraIdentities`, also the undo) and renames them in
the stored candidates; its dialog says "(their tracks)".

**The image check.** Needs the session's videos (else it says why), and the
network the first time, to download the model. An error the embedder marks
`cannotRun` (ui/image-embedder.js — a failed download, or no Nose/TTI) is
returned as the reason rather than thrown, so the status reads "Check ID
Switches (images): needs an internet connection …" (or "skipped — …" after
tracking), not the browser's bare "Failed to fetch" (the original goes to the
console). Without a GPU the embedder runs the model on the CPU
(`pickImageDevice` / `createCpuModelPool`), ~15x slower, and the check runs as
usual — refusing would be wrong for a user who opted in; `fmtDuration`
therefore reaches hours.

Runs under its own cancellable progress dialog (Cancel / Esc -> "cancelled", no
markers added): model download (first use), "Cropping and embedding N views:
frame i of n — about X min left", then "Learning what tells the animals apart
(camera i of n)", with the overall percentage under the bar (embedding is its
first 90%) and the ID Switches rows' playhead line (`.id-switch-phead`) at the
fill's leading edge. Under the percentage, the CPU line over the device line,
and a graph of both loads to their right (`hardware(cpu, gpu)` /
`sample(cpu, gpu)` on the dialog):
- **The CPU line** (`formatEmbedCpu`): the share of ALL
  `navigator.hardwareConcurrency` cores the check kept busy over the last 5 s —
  `createLoadMeter` over (`cpuBusyMs()` + the fit's main-thread time) / cores.
  The fit is timed HERE: it runs synchronously between `progress('fit', …)`
  calls, so the time from the end of one callback's `yieldToPaint` to the start
  of the next is fitting (`fitMs`; the first camera's interval is the wait for
  the last embeddings and is not counted). Video decoding is not in it, and its
  tooltip says so.
- **The device line**: GPU name, backend and dtype, with a load refreshed every
  second (`createLoadMeter` over `busyMs()`); with no GPU, "No GPU: running on
  the CPU (N workers) — slow" in the warning colour and no load.
- **The graph** (`createLoadGraph`, `ui/load-graph.js`, shared with the loading
  overlay): the last `LOAD_GRAPH_SPAN_MS` (60 s), one sample per tick of the
  same 1 s timer, so its newest points ARE the two lines' loads. Points are
  placed by TIME, not index, because fitting blocks the main thread and ticks
  are uneven. The graph and lines reserve their space before they appear, so
  the dialog does not jump. An injected embedder without `device()` shows no
  line.
- **A sample crop** beside them (128 px, captioned with the animal's current
  label and camera): exactly the model input, turned back into pixels by
  `inputTensorToPixels`, so bad keypoints show up as bad crops while the check
  runs — with the skeleton drawn over it (`drawCropSkeleton`, at
  `cropPointsToInput`'s keypoints) on the dialog's canvas only; the model input
  is untouched. It changes every `CROP_PREVIEW_MS` (400 ms), cycling through the
  embedder's `sampleCrops()` by TIME, since crops/s differs ~15x between GPU and
  CPU. An embedder without `sampleCrops` shows no square, nor does the
  coat-brightness check, which shares the dialog but cuts no crops.

The dialog's `finally` also calls the embedder's `releaseFrames` (idempotent):
`checkImageSwitches` does too, but not when it fails before its first frame,
and the CPU workers hold ~600 MB each. On a CPU run "About these flags" says the
model ran on the CPU. Reads `imageCheckHz` (default 2), `imageChangeThreshold`
(default -0.8 — a new id rather than the old `imageCheckThreshold`, because the
image score's scale changed), `imageCheckMaxViews` (default 3, passed as
`maxViewsPerAnimal`) and `imageCheckWebNN` (default 0, as `webnn`), and passes
`inFlight: embedder.inFlight`. The embedder's `backend()` outcome is attached to
the result as `model` (its note shown under "About these flags") and
`embedder.stats()` as `timing` (logged, and shown there as "Image check speed on
this machine: …"). Image rows show their score to one decimal (`fmtScore`; size
rows stay whole numbers). Also reads `imageCheckBackgroundHz` (default 0.1, as
`backgroundHz`) and passes the embedder's `keyframes` and `croppable`, so the check
embeds only the samples its scoring reads plus a sparse background. Time scales with views per animal x `imageCheckHz`
(~9 min for a 30-min, 5-animal session on an M-series Mac).

**User-facing features.** The **ID Switches** tab (`#tabIdSwitches` /
`#idSwitchPanel` in `index.html`, third tab; at the default panel width it is
scrolled off the right of the tab bar, and `openIdSwitchPanel` scrolls it back
into view). Results are stored per session on `session._idSwitch` (`{results:
{size?, image?}, reviewed: Set, fixes, showRepeats, current}`), so they survive
closing/reopening the tab and switching sessions — and are SAVED in the `.slp`
(`metadata.lucid.idSwitchReview`, see `ui/id-switch-review.js`; ticking a row,
a new run and Clear mark the project unsaved) — until the check re-runs (a cue
replaces only its own results; ticks on change points it finds again are kept,
matched by cue + frame + pair), "Clear", or a new tracking pass.

The tab shows a heading, per-check counts, "About these flags" (method,
calibration figures, sampling rate, frame-rate warning, image backend note), a
sticky toolbar ("N of M reviewed", **Next unreviewed ▸** — the next unticked row
after the current one, wrapping — **Clear**, and "Show N later encounters that
still look swapped", only for results restored from files saved before
2026-10-11, since the checks no longer make repeats), then one row per change
point: a **reviewed** checkbox, time, `id_a ↔ id_b` (each name in its
identity's colour), score, the encounter's span with an **end ⇥** button, and
"frame N · check · note". A change point both checks found (same pair,
overlapping contacts) is ONE "Both" row on the earlier check's result (size,
then images, then coat brightness). Ticking a row
dims it and its seekbar tick.

**Where a row lands.** An encounter's frame is the LAST close sample, so a swap
happens before it; clicking a row lands `ID_SWITCH_LEAD_IN_SECONDS` (1 s) before
the close spell STARTS — 2 s on a single-camera session (`idSwitchLeadSeconds`),
where the whole row window (landing frame, progress bar, highlight, the Fix's
current-frame boundary) reaches 2 s either side (`idSwitchLeadInFrame`, from the flag's `startFrame`; its
end frame when the start is unknown, as in older files), so pressing play shows
the whole interaction. **end ⇥** jumps to the end frame; Next unreviewed uses
the lead-in too.

**The selected row's progress bar** follows the viewer's frame: 0% at the
landing frame, the close spell red in the middle, lead-in and lead-out orange,
100% at 1 s after the encounter's end, clamped outside. Section colours come
from `ID_SWITCH_SECTION_RGB` (`ui/id-switch-highlight.js`). A playhead
(`.id-switch-phead`) marks the current frame and stands 4 px proud of the bar,
so the fill is clipped by an inner `.id-switch-ptrack`, not the bar. **Clicking or dragging the bar
goes to that frame** (`round(p0 + x·(p1 − p0))`): a `pointerdown` on the list
with window-level move/up listeners, the bar re-found on every move so a panel
rebuilt mid-drag cannot strand it, and the click that ends the press swallowed
so it never re-lands the row on its lead-in. `updateIdSwitchProgress(frame)` is
called from `ui/ui-wiring.js` `updateSeekbarVisual` on every frame change: one
style write, a no-op without a selected row (the bar element is looked up once
per render/selection, not per frame). The same call drives the **animated box
around the pair in every camera view** (`ui/id-switch-highlight.js`).

A check run from the menu always opens the tab; an automatic one only when it
found something. The tab content is the panel's one scroller. With no results
it says so and offers "Check by body size" / "Check by images…" (they click the
menu items).

**Notes / caveats.** Kept a leaf like `ui/track-range-modal.js` (no import of
`pose/initialization.js`); it opens the panel through the DOM (the toggle button
and the tab button), not by importing ui-wiring. The module keeps its `-modal`
name for the image check's progress dialog, which is still modal.

**Fixing a switch.** The selected row shows **Fix switch…** under its bar. It
opens a confirmation naming the frames (`idSwitchFixPlan`, `ui/id-switch-review.js`)
and why each edge is where it is; Esc / Cancel / a click outside change nothing,
and every keystroke stops at the dialog (capture phase) so no app shortcut acts
under it. Confirming calls `Session.swapIdentitiesInRange` (every view), records
the fix in `st.fixes`, renames the other rows the swap re-labels, ticks the row,
marks the project dirty, repaints through the registered refresher and returns the
view to the row's lead-in. The row then reads **Fixed** with the swapped frames
and, on the LATEST fix only, **Undo fix** — later fixes may build on an earlier
one, so only the last is exactly reversible. A follow-on row's dialog says to
fix the switch it follows first. Re-running a check forgets the fixes (the swaps
stay): its rows were scored on the fixed labels, so an undo would rename them
wrongly.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/progress-delta.js` (`noteProgress`, the
lit stretch on the image check dialog's bar), `ui/app-state.js` (`state`,
`getActiveSession`), `ui/seekbar-markers.js` (`setSeekbarSwitchMarkers`),
`import-export/save-load.js` (`setStatus`, `markDirty`),
`ui/loading-overlay.js` (`showLoading`, `hideLoading`, `yieldToPaint` — the body-size
check shows the overlay's card titled "Checking ID switches (body size)" with a
spinner, not a counter: it is one uninterrupted short block whose counter would
read a frozen "0/1"),
`ui/settings.js` (`getTrackingThreshold`), `pose/id-switch-check.js`,
`pose/single-camera-tracking.js` (`singleCameraName`, `singleCameraCheckSession`,
`swapSingleCameraIdentities`, `singleCameraCheckOptions`),
`ui/image-embedder.js` (`createImageEmbedder`, `IMAGE_MODEL_MB`, `formatEmbedTiming`,
`formatEmbedDevice`, `formatEmbedCpu`, `inputTensorToPixels`, `INPUT`),
`ui/load-graph.js` (`createLoadMeter`, `createLoadGraph`, `setLoadLine`, `cpuCores`),
`ui/browser-hints.js` (`renderBraveFingerprintingHint`: in Brave with its fingerprinting
protection on, the note under the CPU and GPU lines — `prog.braveHint(cores)`),
`ui/id-switch-review.js` (row keys, change-point helpers, `linkIdSwitchResults`,
`idSwitchFixPlan`, `idSwitchFixFor`, `idSwitchRenameForFix`),
`ui/id-switch-highlight.js` (`setIdSwitchHighlight`, `updateIdSwitchHighlight`,
`refreshIdSwitchHighlight`, `ID_SWITCH_SECTION_RGB`).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`#menuCheckSizeSwitches`,
`#menuCheckImageSwitches`, `setIdSwitchNavigator`, `setIdSwitchRefresher`, `updateIdSwitchProgress`), `pose/tracker.js` (the
automatic run, `clearIdSwitchResults`), `ui/info-panel.js` and
`ui/sessions-panes.js` (`refreshIdSwitchPanel`), `ui/track-summary-modal.js`
(`openIdSwitchPanel`, `refreshIdSwitchPanel`, `setTrackSummaryOpener`, `clickCheckFromSummary`).

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/size-switch-check.mjs` (size, menu path, the checklist
and its persistence, Clear, one scroller),
`tests/e2e/track-auto-size-switch-check.mjs` (after Track All / Track Frame Range),
`tests/e2e/id-switch-image-check.mjs` (images with an injected embedder: "Both"
merge, Esc cancel, the progress dialog's lines, graph and sample crop, the
no-GPU CPU run, default off), `tests/e2e/image-check-cpu.mjs` (the real
dialog's measured CPU line and CPU-only graph), `tests/e2e/id-switch-fix.mjs`
(Fix switch…: the dialog, the swap, the renamed follow-on, saved, Undo exact).

---

### ui/id-switch-review.js

**Purpose.** The ID Switches tab's review checklist as data: the helpers that read
a session's check results (`session._idSwitch`) and their `.slp` serialization,
`metadata.lucid.idSwitchReview`, written and read through
`import-export/visibility-metadata.js` (the one `metadata.lucid` seam).

**Key exports.** `idSwitchRowKey(m)` (check + frame + identity NAMES — names, not
ids, are what a reopened project still agrees on); `idSwitchPrimary(res)` (change
points), `idSwitchMarkers(res)` (+ repeats), `idSwitchOnsets(res)` (the
"possible switches" count), `idSwitchEncounterCount(res)`;
`linkIdSwitchResults(results)` (tag each marker's `cue`; link each change point of
a later check to the nearest unlinked one of an earlier check of the same pair at
the same contact, as `agree` — "Both": their close spells overlap, give or take
1 s; or, without start frames, they end within 1 s); `ID_SWITCH_CUES`
(`['size', 'image', 'brightness', 'review']`, the order of precedence — 'review' is
single-camera Track All's review list, unscored rows (score 0, `look` = cues) saved
under `checks.review` with no image fields; last, so a check's row at the same
place is the one shown);
`idSwitchIsSecondary(m)` (the later half of a "Both" pair — listed and drawn
through its partner); `idSwitchIsPlace(m)` (a row that stands for a place, not a
scored encounter: a review-list row, or a check's row at a moment, `look`);
`idSwitchPlaceOf(m)` (the place a tab row stands for: a review-list place first —
itself or its linked row — else itself or its linked row when at a moment; null);
`serializeIdSwitchReview(session)` -> payload or `null` (no check results -> no
key, so untouched projects keep their bytes); `ingestIdSwitchReview(session,
payload)` (rebuilds `session._idSwitch`, re-links "Both"; ignores anything
malformed, never throws); `idSwitchFixPlan(m, res, {currentFrame,
totalFrames, window?, place?, results?})` -> `{key, partnerKey, nameA, nameB, from, to, start
('current' | 'separate'), edge}` or null
— what fixing row `m` swaps (see below); `idSwitchFixFor(st, m)` (the fix covering
a row, its own or its partner's); `idSwitchRenameForFix(st, fix)` (renames the
other pairs' rows inside a fixed stretch — an involution, keys follow).

**What a fix swaps.** The boundary is the CURRENT frame whenever it is inside the
row's window (`o.window` — in the app the whole progress bar), so the user puts
it where they saw the labels flip; outside the window it falls back to e + 1
(where the animals separate). Without `o.window` the window is the close spell
[s, e + 1]. A place's own fix (`place`, always for a review-list row) swaps from
the boundary to the END of the video, with no partner, and its window reaches on to
just before the next place (`nextPlaceStart`; the end of the video after the last),
so a swap that undoes itself between two places is fixed from that row twice. The
next place is the next row standing for one on ANY list (`results`, else `res`),
not counting the row itself, its linked row, or a place of the same pair at the same
contact on another list (the same place, listed twice). An onset swaps from there to its `switchBackAt` encounter's last
close frame, or the last frame; an 'end' swaps the stretch BEFORE it, from just
after `switchedAt` (or frame 0) to the frame before its boundary. The change point
at the other edge is the fix's `partnerKey`. Results restored from a file saved
before the links existed pair by the nearest change point of the same pair on
the right side. After a fix, every OTHER row in the stretch that names exactly
one of the pair is about the animal that now carries the other name, so it is
renamed (selecting it must still box the same animals); the fixed pair's own
rows are not.

**Format.** `{v: 1, checks: {size?|image?: {encounters, sampleHz, step, fps,
fpsFromVideo, [imageHz, crops, cameras, model: {name, note}], points: [[frame,
nameA, nameB, score (0.1), kind ('' | 'end'), followOf (frame | -1), continues
(0 | 1), startFrame (the encounter's first close frame | -1; absent in older
files — rows then land on the end frame), link (`switchBackAt` / `switchedAt` |
-1 for none; absent in older files), look (a candidate moment's cues, e.g.
`['tracklet']`; written ONLY for a point scored at a moment, so a project
without single-camera moments saves byte-identically)], …]}},
reviewed: [rowKey, …], fixes?: [[key, partnerKey, nameA, nameB, from, to], …]}`
(`fixes` oldest first, only when something was fixed) — only what the tab and the timeline
draw; not the encounters or the fitted models. A restored check result has
`restored: true` and `encounterCount` instead of `encounters`.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/id-switch-modal.js`, `ui/track-summary.js`
(`idSwitchOnsets`, `idSwitchEncounterCount`), `import-export/visibility-metadata.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-id-switch-check.mjs` (lossless reopen -> re-save,
"Both" re-link, ticks, garbage tolerance, nothing written without results);
`tests/test-id-switch-fix.mjs` (the links, the plans, renaming, fixes round trip,
and §6 a candidate moment's row taking over its stretch);
`tests/e2e/visibility-settings-roundtrip.mjs` (both writers, real reader).

---

### ui/id-switch-highlight.js

**Purpose.** An animated box around the selected ID-switch row's two animals in
every camera view, while the viewer's frame is inside that row's interval (1 s
before they come close -> 1 s after they separate — the row's progress-bar span).

**Key exports.** `setIdSwitchHighlight({nameA, nameB, p0, s, e, p1} | null)`
(`s..e` is the close spell); `updateIdSwitchHighlight(frame)` (every frame
change, via `ui/id-switch-modal.js` `updateIdSwitchProgress`);
`refreshIdSwitchHighlight()` (recompute the boxes at the current frame after the
identities changed — a fix — or the node weights did, on Settings Apply); `getIdSwitchHighlight()`; `ID_SWITCH_SECTION_RGB` (`{lead, close}` as `r, g, b`
strings — orange / red) and `idSwitchSection(target, frame)` (`'close'` over
`[s, e]`, else `'lead'`); `idSwitchBoxNodeMask(nodeNames)` (`mask[k]` true for a
node weighted above 0, or `null` = every node when none or all are weighted 0).

**How.** Draws on its OWN canvas per view (`.id-switch-canvas`, appended to the
view's `.canvas-wrapper`, `pointer-events: none`), sized like the overlay
canvas: the wrapper's CSS transform carries zoom/pan/rotation, the overlay redraw
paths never touch it, and the overlay-video export does not include it. A
`requestAnimationFrame` loop runs ONLY while the frame is in the interval (the
outline marches and pulses even when paused) and stops after clearing the
canvases once outside it. Kept cheap because it runs during playback on every
view: repaint on a frame change, else at most every `ANIM_MS` (33 ms; the
animation is clock-timed), clearing only the previously drawn rectangle
(`_dirty`).

Boxes are recomputed only when the frame changes: per camera, the instances
whose identity NAME is one of the pair at that frame (per-frame track identity
first, then the group's `identityId`; unlinked instances via
`getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance`), one box around both (or the one visible),
labelled "id_a ↔ id_b" in the identities' colours. **The box encloses only the
nodes the Tracking Wizard weights above 0** (`getNodeWeightArray`), so with tail
nodes at 0 it frames bodies, not a sweeping tail. An instance with no weighted
node visible in a view falls back to ALL its nodes, so an animal is never
dropped. Settings Apply calls `refreshIdSwitchHighlight`, so a weight change
shows on the frame already on screen. The outline wears the colour of the
progress-bar SECTION the frame is in, from `ID_SWITCH_SECTION_RGB`, which the
bar (`ui/id-switch-modal.js` `progressHtml`) also reads, so the two cannot
drift. Lines, padding and text are sized in SCREEN pixels, so a small tile of a
large video stays readable.

Lazy projects: nothing for a non-resident frame. Selecting a row usually jumps
to a non-resident frame, and its hydration redraws without being a frame change,
so while the frame is missing (`_waiting`) the animation loop checks
`frameGroups.has(frame)` on each tick and recomputes when it lands.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/app-state.js` (`state`), `ui/overlays.js`
(`makeVideoToCanvasTransform`), `ui/settings.js` (`getNodeWeightArray`).

**Imported by.** `ui/id-switch-modal.js`, `ui/settings-modal.js`
(`refreshIdSwitchHighlight`, on Apply).

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/id-switch-highlight.mjs` (two real views: the box
around the pair only, animation while paused, section colours matching the
bar, the bar's playhead and seeking, clearing outside the interval, Clear, node
weights and their fallback, and a non-resident landing frame boxed once
hydrated); `tests/e2e/_bench-playback.mjs` scenario `idswitch` (playback cost
with a row selected).

---

### ui/image-embedder.js

**Purpose.** Appearance embeddings for the image ID-switch check: for a sampled
frame and the identities present, decode that frame in every camera
(streamed, and moved to the nearest keyframe when keyframes are dense: see
below), cut a masked, pose-aligned crop of each identity from
its own 2D keypoints, and embed the crops with DINOv2-small — on the GPU, or
without one on the CPU.

**Where the model runs** (`pickImageDevice`, at `createImageEmbedder`): a HARDWARE
WebGPU adapter, else the CPU — no WebGPU, no adapter, or only a software one.
A software adapter (SwiftShader, what a VM with no GPU gets) ran 10x slower
than ONE CPU worker, so it is never used.
**The CPU path** runs the fp32 model on WebAssembly in module workers
(`createCpuModelPool`, `ui/image-model-worker.js`), each with its own model:
- **Same embeddings.** CPU fp32 matches WebGPU fp32 (cosine 1.00000). The CPU
  runtime's DEFAULT model is int8, which is what drifts from the calibration —
  the dtype, not the device.
- **Off the main thread, or the page freezes** (one run blocks for seconds,
  stalling the dialog and its Cancel). onnxruntime's own `wasm.proxy` worker
  cannot start from the CDN bundle, so LUCID runs its own.
- **Several workers, because one thread each.** Without cross-origin isolation
  (which GitHub Pages cannot turn on) WebAssembly gets ONE thread; workers scale
  roughly linearly to 6. Each costs ~600 MB, so `cpuModelWorkerCount` takes half
  the cores beyond two, at most one per 2 GB of `navigator.deviceMemory`, at most
  `CPU_MODEL_MAX_WORKERS` (4), at least 1.
- The first worker downloads the model (88 MB, reported) and the rest load it
  from the browser cache; a worker that fails to start is dropped.
  `run(data, n)` splits a batch evenly over the workers, in order.
  `releaseFrames` terminates them — they are not kept between runs.
WebNN is not tried on the CPU path (it is judged against WebGPU). The run
summary says "model busy" and "CPU (N workers) fp32" instead of "GPU busy" /
"WebGPU".

**Speed.** `prepareFrames(frames, items, gridFrames)` opens one `streamingReader` per
camera over the round's sorted frame list (mediabunny `samplesAtTimestamps`: decode forward once,
instead of `getFrame` re-decoding from the keyframe for every sample; falls back
to `getFrame` without a mediabunny backend); `releaseFrames()` closes them. The check embeds only the samples its scoring reads
plus a sparse background (`planImageSamples`, pose/id-switch-check.js), so readers
jump over keyframe gaps holding no planned sample; the provider helps it plan:
`keyframes()` (per view, `keyframeIndices`: where the background is cheapest) and
`croppable(frames, items, gridFrames)` (per frame, per item: does any view have crop
geometry? `frameCropGeometry` at the frames each view decodes — from the keypoints,
before decoding — so a plan needs one pass). `prepareFrames` may be called again for a
later pass (it closes the previous readers); `stats()` adds the passes up.
Model runs are serialised on a queue, so the check's one-ahead request decodes
and crops the next frame while the GPU embeds the current one.
`maxViewsPerAnimal` (from `imageCheckMaxViews`) embeds only each animal's N
largest views (`selectViews`, by Nose–TTI pixel length, decided from the
keypoints before decoding); cameras no animal needs are not fetched.
Cropping runs in a pool of module workers (`createCropPool`,
`ui/image-crop-worker.js`; one per core but one, at most 8; one job = one view of
one frame, its VideoFrame transferred and closed by the worker — a decoder-cached
frame is sent as a `clone()`, anything else as an ImageBitmap copy). A failed
worker drops that one view of that frame and the rest of the run crops inline;
`window.LUCID_CROP_WORKERS = 0` forces inline. `writeInputTensor`'s 160 -> 224
sample positions are a precomputed table (same arithmetic, bit-identical).
**GPU feeding.** (1) The model's output stays on the GPU
(`preferredOutputLocation: 'gpu-buffer'`; its ONLY output is `last_hidden_state`
[n, 257, 384]) and `clsFromGpu` copies just token 0 of each crop into a staging
buffer — bit-identical, 1/257 of the readback. (2) Model runs are a greedy BATCH
QUEUE: one run at a time, each taking every crop queued meanwhile (up to
`EMBED_MAX_BATCH` = 64), with the check keeping `EMBED_IN_FLIGHT` = 8 frames in
flight (`inFlight` on the provider) so batches fill while the GPU works (M2 Pro:
GPU busy 66% -> 98%, embeddings identical). (3) `stats()` /
`summarizeEmbedTiming` / `formatEmbedTiming`: crops/s, GPU busy %, batches,
ms/crop, and per-frame decode / crop / queue latency — logged and shown under
"About these flags", so a slow run says where it waited, ending with the backend
and precision that ran ("· WebGPU fp16" — fp32 where the GPU lacks
`shader-f16`).
**Live, in the progress dialog** (`device()` / `busyMs()` -> `formatEmbedDevice`
+ `createLoadMeter`; `cpuBusyMs()` -> `formatEmbedCpu`). `cpuBusyMs()` is one
`createBusyClock` (`ui/load-graph.js`) shared by the crop pool, the CPU model
pool and inline crops (a lane per worker, busy while it HOLDS a job); it
outlives the pools (the crop pool is re-made after `releaseFrames`).
`gpuAdapterInfo(device, adapter)` names the GPU from the model's own
`GPUDevice.adapterInfo` (onnxruntime's `env.webgpu.adapter` is undefined in this
build) and reads `isFallbackAdapter`: a SOFTWARE adapter is WebGPU on the CPU,
so `pickImageDevice` sends it to the CPU workers and the line says "No GPU". The
load is the share of the last `GPU_LOAD_WINDOW_MS` (5 s) spent in model runs;
other apps' GPU use is not in it (no browser API reports it), and its tooltip
says so. WebNN is not called a GPU (the browser picks its device).
**Decode workers were tried and removed** (no gain on an M2 Pro, half speed on
a Windows RTX VM). Cheaper decoding has to come from the recordings (dense
keyframes — see below).
**Keyframe sampling.** `prepareFrames` reads each camera's keyframes from the
container's packet index (`keyframeIndices`: mediabunny
`EncodedPacketSink.packets(…, {metadataOnly: true})`, `type === 'key'`, mapped to
frame indices through the backend's `_frameTimes`; no frame data read, cached per
video) and plans the samples with `planKeyframeSamples` (pose/id-switch-check.js) —
over EVERY image sample (`gridFrames`), asked for or not, so a sample decodes the
frame it would in a run that embeds them all (same crop, same cache key; `planDecode`
is shared with `croppable`):
when the median keyframe gap is <= `KEYFRAME_GAP_TOLERANCE` (1.1) x the sample
spacing (the 10% lets a recorder's "keyframe every 0.5 s" qualify where rounding
makes the spacing a little shorter), each sample moves to its nearest keyframe
within half of max(spacing, keyframe gap) that has tracking
(`session.instanceGroups.has`), so `samplesAtTimestamps` decodes ONE frame per
sample. Each view crops its animals from the keypoints of the frame it actually
decoded (`decodedFrame`; an identity seen twice in that frame is left out),
while the evidence still counts at the grid frame, so encounters and tracklets
are unchanged. Sparser keyframes move nothing. Per camera, so cameras encoded
differently mix. `opts.keyframes`: `false` (or `window.
LUCID_IMAGE_KEYFRAMES = 0`) turns it off; a function `(decoder) -> keyframe
indices` replaces the index (diagnostics). `stats().keyframes` /
`summarizeKeyframePlans` -> `{cameras, of, snappedPct, keyframeGap, spacing}`,
reported at the end of the speed line (and, when keyframes are too sparse, what
spacing would let it snap). With a keyframe every 30 frames it decodes ~18x
faster than with one every 250 (`tests/e2e/_bench-image-keyframe-decode.mjs`),
and a keyframe decoded alone is bit-identical to it decoded mid-stream; the cost
is file size (~+50% for HEVC).
Embeddings are bit-identical across all of this (seeking vs streaming, top-k vs
all views, worker vs inline crops).

**Repeat runs: the embedding cache** (`pose/embedding-cache.js`, one module-level
LRU shared by all sessions). A crop is a pure function of the video, the camera,
the frame decoded for that view and how (`'s'` streamed or `'g'` `getFrame` — the
two hand the crop a different kind of image), the animal's crop geometry and the
hulls masked out of it; its vector also of the model. So each vector is stored
under `<session uid>|<backend/dtype that produced it>|<view>|<frame decoded>|<s|g>|
<cropKeyHash of the geometry and of the masks that reach the crop (hullsReachingCrop)>`
(`planFrameCrops` builds the key body; the producer's tag rides on
each batch's result). `embeddingCacheScope(session, views)` gives the session's
prefix and drops the entries of sessions no longer in `state.sessions` (closed or
reloaded projects) and of this session when its videos changed (`viewVideoId`:
the decoder's source File / URL object, else the decoder). `prepareFrames(frames,
items, gridFrames)` plans the keyframe snapping over ALL image samples first (so a
sample decodes the frame it always would), then — only when this session + model has stored anything,
so a first run does no extra work — computes every frame's crops and looks them up:
a frame whose crops are all cached is answered by `getEmbeddings` without
geometry, decode, crop or model, and is left out of the streaming readers (a
camera with nothing left to decode gets no reader); a partly cached frame decodes
only the cameras with a missing crop and embeds only those crops. Hits are copied
out at prepare time, so a mid-run eviction cannot turn a planned hit into a miss.
Results are assembled per item in view order (`assembleItems`) — the order the
model path always returned — and a hit carries `cached: true`. Not used while a
WebNN trial is undecided (its backend may still switch); `opts.cache: false` or
`window.LUCID_IMAGE_CACHE = 0` turns it off. `stats()` adds `cachedCrops`,
`cachedFrames`, `cacheLookupMs`; the speed line ends "· N of M crops (P%) from
the cache, F frames not decoded" (or "all N crops from the cache" when the model
never ran). **Sample crops on cached runs:** a frame answered from the cache cuts
no crop, so each run keeps the grey pixels (`tensorToGrey`, INPUT² bytes) of the
dialog's sample crop on `SAMPLE_FRAMES_PER_RUN` (200) evenly spaced frames, under
that crop's cache key (at most `SAMPLE_PIXELS_MAX` = 256 kept, 12.8 MB, dropped with
the vectors); when such a frame comes from the cache, `sampleCrops()` gets that
crop back (`greyToTensor`: channel 0 only, all `inputTensorToPixels` reads, so the
dialog shows the same bytes), marked `cached: true`, with the skeleton overlay
from today's keypoints (`cropPointsToInput` of the current geometry — the tail is
not in the key).

**WebNN (opt-in, experimental: `webnn: true`, from `imageCheckWebNN`).** WebNN can
reach hardware WebGPU cannot (on Windows, DirectML on NVIDIA tensor cores). It is
behind `chrome://flags/#web-machine-learning-neural-network`, compiles a static
graph (input fixed to `WEBNN_BATCH` = 8 crops via `freeDimensionOverrides`, the
last batch zero-padded), and its numerics are not the calibrated model's. So the
first `WEBNN_TRIAL_FRAMES` (6) frames are embedded on BOTH backends — WebGPU's
output is what the check uses — and `chooseBackend` keeps WebNN only if it is
>= 10% faster and agrees (median cosine >= 0.998, worst >= 0.98); it stops early
once WebNN runs at under half WebGPU's speed. Any WebNN failure falls back to
WebGPU. `backend()` -> `{name, note}` says what happened. On macOS WebNN runs on
the CPU, so the trial keeps WebGPU. Forced WebNN (`webnn: 'force'`) is for
benchmarking only. WebNN runs return results on the CPU, so run + readback are
one figure in the speed line.

**Offline, or a host blocked (`asModelDownloadFailure`).** The runtime
(cdn.jsdelivr.net) and the model (huggingface.co) download on first use, and
without the network both reject with a bare TypeError naming no cause, worded
differently per browser. Every such wording, from either download (and
onnxruntime's own failed `.wasm` fetch, which it wraps in a plain Error),
becomes `MODEL_OFFLINE_REASON` as a `cannotRun` error, which `runImage`
(ui/id-switch-modal.js) reports as the reason the check cannot run rather than
a failure. Anything else is left alone. The CPU workers classify it themselves,
where the error is still a real one, and post the flag across. **A retry needs
no reload** (`importTransformers`): Chrome keeps a failed dynamic import of a URL
failed for the page's lifetime, so the runtime is imported once, and a retry
after a failure imports it under a new FRAGMENT (`#retry1`) — the same request,
since a fragment is never sent, but a new module-map entry. `loadImageModel` and
`loadWebNNModel` share it, so the page never holds two copies of the runtime.
`createImageEmbedder`'s two preflight throws (no videos, no Nose/TTI) are
`cannotRun` errors too.

**Key exports.** `createImageEmbedder(session, {onStatus, maxViewsPerAnimal, webnn, keyframes, cache, model})` ->
`{getEmbeddings, prepareFrames, keyframes, croppable, releaseFrames, backend, device, busyMs, cpuBusyMs, stats, inFlight, views}` (the provider
`checkImageSwitches` needs; `releaseFrames` also terminates the crop pool and the
CPU model workers and disposes a WebNN model; `device()` -> `{backend: 'webgpu'|'webnn'|'cpu',
comparing, dtype, adapter, fallback, workers, why}` and `busyMs()` feed the progress
dialog's device line, `sampleCrops()` its sample crop: `[{tensor, frame, camera,
identityId, points}]` (`points` from `cropPointsToInput`), one per frame rotating
through its animals and cameras, the last `SAMPLE_CROPS` kept as REFERENCES
(nothing is converted unless the dialog asks) — several rather than the latest
because on the CPU frames arrive in bursts); `opts.device` ('auto' | 'webgpu' |
'cpu') and `opts.cpuWorkers` force a device / worker count (tests, benchmarking);
`opts.model` (tests, benchmarks) replaces the model with an async `(data, n) -> n vectors`
(backend 'test'); `embeddingCacheScope(session, views)`, `viewVideoId(view)`,
`clearEmbeddingCache()`, `embeddingCacheInfo()` -> `{entries, bytes, maxEntries, samples}` (the cache, above);
`SAMPLE_FRAMES_PER_RUN`, `SAMPLE_PIXELS_MAX`, `tensorToGrey(tensor)`, `greyToTensor(grey)` (sample crops on cached runs);
`pickImageDevice()` -> `{kind: 'webgpu'|'cpu', adapter, why}`; `cpuModelWorkerCount(cores, memoryGB)`,
`CPU_MODEL_MAX_WORKERS`; `createCpuModelPool(count, onStatus, clock?)` -> `{size, run(data, n) ->
Promise<Float32Array[]>, terminate()}`; `createCropPool(clock?)` -> `{broken, run(image, crops), terminate()}` | null
(`clock`: a `createBusyClock`, ui/load-graph.js);
`loadImageModel(onStatus)` (WebGPU; once, cached promise);
`importTransformers()` (the runtime, imported once; re-imported under a new fragment
after a failure); `cannotRun(reason, cause)` (an Error whose message is the reason
the check cannot run, flagged `cannotRun`); `asModelDownloadFailure(err)` (a failed
download as `MODEL_OFFLINE_REASON`, else `err` unchanged); `MODEL_OFFLINE_REASON`;
`selectViews(geos, maxViews)`; `EMBED_MAX_BATCH`, `EMBED_IN_FLIGHT`,
`summarizeEmbedTiming(tm, backend, dtype)`, `formatEmbedTiming(t)`;
`describeAdapter(info)`, `gpuAdapterInfo(device, adapter)` -> `{name, fallback}`,
`createLoadMeter` (re-exported from ui/load-graph.js, where it now lives), `GPU_LOAD_WINDOW_MS` (=
`LOAD_WINDOW_MS`; the CPU line's window too),
`formatEmbedDevice(device, load)` -> `{text, warn, title, metered, load}` (`metered`: the line has a series in
the load graph — not for the CPU workers or a software adapter; `load`: what it shows, null when none),
`formatEmbedCpu(cores, load)` -> `{text, title, load}` (`formatCpuLoad` with this check's "counts": cropping,
the CPU model, fitting — not video decoding); `inputTensorToPixels(tensor, rgba)`
(a model input back to grey RGBA, the inverse of `writeInputTensor`'s normalisation),
`SAMPLE_CROPS` (12); `cropPointsToInput(g)` (`g.pts`, the keypoints `cropGeometry` also
records, NaN when missing, into model-input pixels: cutCrop's rotate-about-the-body-centre
and scale, then x 224/160 — exact for an align_corners=false resize; pinned
against `cutCrop` itself within 0.04 px, at any rotation);
`keyframeIndices(decoder)` -> `Promise<Int32Array|null>` (cached per video);
`summarizeKeyframePlans(plans)`; WebNN: `hasWebNN()`, `loadWebNNModel(onStatus)`,
`chooseBackend(trial)`, `WEBNN_BATCH`, `WEBNN_TRIAL_FRAMES`; crop helpers
`cropGeometry` (also `pts`: every keypoint, for the overlay — cutCrop ignores it), `cutCrop`,
`convexHull`, `writeInputTensor`, `skeletonIndex(nodes)` (also `n`, the node count; Nose and TTI found regardless
of case through `pose/id-switch-check.js` `findNode`, the body = every node
`isTailNode` does not call tail),
`frameCropGeometry(session, frame, items, cams, atFrames, sk)` -> `geo[view][item]`, built on
`mapFrameInstances(session, frame, items, cams, atFrames, fn)` and `streamingReader(decoder,
frames, decode)` — both shared with `ui/brightness-sampler.js`; constants
`TRANSFORMERS_URL`, `IMAGE_MODEL_ID`, `IMAGE_MODEL_MB`, `CROP` (160), `INPUT` (224).

**The crop** (must match the calibration): rotate so the nose points right
(Nose − TTI), centre on the mean of the body keypoints, side 1.3 x body length,
160 x 160, greyscale; outside the animal's convex hull dilated by a quarter body
length is black, and so are the other animals' hulls in that view; resized to
224 (bilinear) and ImageNet-normalised; embedding = CLS token of
`last_hidden_state` (post-layernorm). Checked against the offline pipeline on
real crops: embeddings cosine 0.971 (vs 0.644 between different crops).

**Crop geometry on a lazy project: `frameCropGeometry`.** The geometry comes from
group members' 2D, for frames the check never makes resident — and on a lazy
project those members hold no 2D (`pose/lazy-residency.js`), so a direct read
yields no geometry. `frameCropGeometry` hydrates the members of each frame it
reads (the sampled frame and any keyframe-snapped `atFrames`) with
`hydrateFrameMembers2d`, computes the geometry, and gives the 2D back with
`releaseFrameMembers2d` — so the check costs no residency.

**Dependencies.** transformers.js **pinned to 4.3.0** (`/+esm` from jsdelivr —
pinned for the reason dockview is) and `onnx-community/dinov2-small` from the
Hugging Face CDN, both fetched on FIRST USE and cached by the browser; nothing
about the user's data is sent. fp16 (~44 MB) when the GPU has `shader-f16`, else
fp32 (~88 MB). Without a hardware GPU the fp32 model runs on the CPU in workers
— never the CPU runtime's default int8 model, which drifts (cosine 0.953 vs the
calibrated model). Re-check the CLS extraction (`last_hidden_state` token 0) on
any version bump.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/app-state.js` (`state.views`, `state.sessions`),
`pose/id-switch-check.js` (`planKeyframeSamples`, `KEYFRAME_GAP_TOLERANCE`),
`pose/embedding-cache.js` (`EmbeddingCache`, `cropKeyHash`),
`pose/lazy-residency.js` (`hydrateFrameMembers2d`, `releaseFrameMembers2d` — DOM-free,
so the crop worker can load it), `ui/load-graph.js` (`createLoadMeter`,
`createBusyClock`, `formatCpuLoad`, `LOAD_WINDOW_MS`); `mediabunny`
(`EncodedPacketSink`, imported LAZILY inside `keyframeIndices` — a static bare
import would break this module in Node tests and in the crop worker, which has no
importmap). Spawns `ui/image-crop-worker.js` and `ui/image-model-worker.js`.

**Imported by.** `ui/id-switch-modal.js`, `ui/image-crop-worker.js`,
`ui/image-model-worker.js`, `ui/brightness-sampler.js`.

**Coverage.** Crop geometry, `selectViews`, `chooseBackend`, the resize table,
`formatEmbedTiming`'s keyframe line and the dialog's lines in
`tests/test-id-switch-check.mjs` (the shared load helpers:
`tests/test-load-graph.mjs`); `frameCropGeometry` on a released member in
`tests/test-lazy-residency.mjs`; the crop pool and its busy clock in
`tests/e2e/image-crop-worker.mjs`; `cpuBusyMs()` over the real CPU model workers
in `tests/e2e/image-check-cpu.mjs`; keyframe sampling on generated H.264 and AV1
in `tests/e2e/image-keyframe-sampling.mjs`; a failed download in
`tests/test-image-model-download.mjs` and `tests/e2e/image-check-offline.mjs`
(every off-localhost request aborted after boot, GPU and CPU paths, a retry in
the same page); the embedding cache in `tests/e2e/image-check-cache.mjs` (the
real embedder over streamed fake videos and a pixel-sensitive stand-in model:
cache on = off, a warm run decodes and embeds nothing, one moved keypoint re-embeds
exactly the crops whose key changed, leaving unreachable hulls out of the key is
lossless, a cached run's sample crops are the crops' own pixels and the real dialog
shows one); accuracy on real data by
`tests/e2e/_diag-image-keyframe-snap.mjs`, the cache's speed and equality on real
data by `tests/e2e/_bench-image-cache.mjs` (both diagnostic, not in the suite).

---

### ui/brightness-sampler.js

**Purpose.** The vector provider behind Tracks ▸ **Check ID Switches (Coat
Brightness)…** (`pose/id-switch-check.js` `checkBrightnessSwitches`): for each
animal in each view, how bright its coat is. Reads the mean grey level (0.299 R +
0.587 G + 0.114 B, as the image crops) in a disc at every body (non-tail)
keypoint — radius 1/35 of the animal's body extent, 1–8 px (4 px for the ~140 px
mice it was calibrated on) — and summarises them as the 10th / 50th / 90th
percentile and the mean, log-scaled. Percentiles over whatever body nodes the
skeleton has, so no node names are needed; missing and occluded keypoints are
skipped, and fewer than 4 left gives no vector there. No model and no GPU: the
only cost is decoding the video, which it streams per view exactly as the image
check does (`streamingReader`, keyframe plans) — about 24 s for a 10-min,
4-mouse, one-camera HEVC video at the default 4 samples/s.

**Why a separate cue from images.** DINOv2 is trained to be invariant to
brightness, which under IR is exactly what tells white, brown and black mice
apart; this measures it directly. On the proofread single-camera SLAP videos its
candidate-moment rows caught 14 real switches with no false rows (the image
check's: 13; 12 shared), without a model download or WebGPU.

**Key exports.** `createBrightnessSampler(session, {keyframes?})` ->
`{getEmbeddings(frame, items), prepareFrames, releaseFrames, inFlight, views,
stats}` (throws without loaded videos or with fewer than 4 body nodes);
`brightnessFeatures(values)`; `bodyPoints(inst, bodyIdx)` -> `{pts, r}` | null;
`discMean(px, w, h, x0, y0, x, y, r)`; `sampleAnimals(image, animals, canvas)`
(one read of the region covering every animal's discs); `discRadius(extent)`;
`BRIGHTNESS_QUANTILES`, `MIN_BRIGHTNESS_POINTS`, `BRIGHTNESS_IN_FLIGHT`.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/app-state.js` (`state.views`),
`ui/image-embedder.js` (`skeletonIndex`, `mapFrameInstances`, `streamingReader`,
`keyframeIndices`), `pose/id-switch-check.js` (`planKeyframeSamples`).

**Imported by.** `ui/id-switch-modal.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-id-switch-check.mjs` (the pure helpers, and the check on
synthetic coats catching a swap between equal-sized animals that the size check
misses, and the candidate moments it tests) and `tests/e2e/single-camera-track-all.mjs`
§4/§5 (the check in the real tab, with a stand-in sampler: a moment's row, and a
"Both" row with the size check); on real data by `tests/e2e/_real-single-camera.mjs`.

---

### ui/image-crop-worker.js

**Purpose.** Module worker that cuts the image ID-switch check's crops off the
main thread, for `createCropPool` in `ui/image-embedder.js`. It runs the same
`cutCrop` + `writeInputTensor` the main thread would, so its output is
bit-identical (asserted).

**Messages.** IN `{id, image: VideoFrame|ImageBitmap (transferred), crops:
[{g: cropGeometry, others: [hull]}]}` — one camera view of one frame; OUT `{id,
tensors: [Float32Array(3 x 224 x 224)]}` (transferred, one per crop, in order) or
`{id, error}`. The worker owns the image and closes it.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/image-embedder.js` (`cutCrop`,
`writeInputTensor`, `CROP`, `INPUT`; that module's static imports —
`ui/app-state.js`, `pose/id-switch-check.js`, `pose/lazy-residency.js`,
`ui/load-graph.js` — must stay worker-safe, which is why it imports
`mediabunny` lazily).

**Spawned by.** `ui/image-embedder.js` (`createCropPool`).

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/image-crop-worker.mjs`.

---

### ui/image-model-worker.js

**Purpose.** Module worker that runs the image ID-switch check's model on the CPU
when there is no hardware GPU: DINOv2-small at **fp32** on WebAssembly (the
default int8 is what drifts from the calibration; fp32 matches the WebGPU fp32
model). Off the main thread because there a run blocks the page for seconds;
several run side by side because WebAssembly gets one thread without
cross-origin isolation. See
`ui/image-embedder.js` ("The CPU path") for the measurements.

**Messages.** IN `{type: 'load'}` -> `{type: 'loaded'}` (while downloading:
`{type: 'progress', loaded, total}`) or `{type: 'error', message, cannotRun}`
(`cannotRun`: a failed download, `message` then being `MODEL_OFFLINE_REASON` —
classified here by `asModelDownloadFailure`, where the error is still a real
one); IN `{type:
'run', id, data: Float32Array(n x 3 x 224 x 224) (transferred), n}` -> `{type:
'result', id, cls: Float32Array(n x 384) (transferred), dim}` — each crop's CLS
token, read exactly as the main thread's `clsVectors` does — or `{type: 'error',
id, message}`.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/image-embedder.js` (`importTransformers`,
`asModelDownloadFailure`, `IMAGE_MODEL_ID`, `INPUT` — so the pin stays in one
place), then the runtime through `importTransformers` (a dynamic cross-origin
import of `TRANSFORMERS_URL`, which a module worker may do).

**Spawned by.** `ui/image-embedder.js` (`createCpuModelPool`).

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/image-check-cpu.mjs` (headless Chromium has no GPU;
vectors bit-identical to the main thread's), `tests/e2e/id-switch-image-check.mjs`
§4, `tests/e2e/image-check-offline.mjs`.

---

### ui/settings.js

**Purpose.** Central user-settings store: the default triangulation method
(`'dlt'` | `'ba'`, default `'dlt'`), per-skeleton-node **tracking weights**
(name → weight in `[0,1]`, default `1`), per-camera **tracking inclusion**
(name → `0|1`, default `1` = view participates in tracking; `0` = excluded from
the association math but still shown in the GUI), the cross-view **tracking thresholds**
(`TRACKING_THRESHOLDS` catalog — Tier A scoring knobs, Tier B reprojection gates,
and the benchmark-derived levers `track3dWeight` / `filterMinVisibleNodes` /
`filterMinInstanceScore`; see `pose/tracker.js`), plus a comprehensive **catalog
of every keyboard shortcut**
(`ACTION_CATALOG`). Settings persist to `localStorage` (`lucid.settings.v1`) and
survive reloads. The catalog is the single source of truth for the Settings ▸
Keyboard Shortcuts panel — see the keyboard-shortcuts note in `CLAUDE.md`.

**Catalog entries.** `{ id, label, category, binding, editable, dispatched }`.
`binding` is a "+"-joined accelerator (modifier tokens: `Mod` = Ctrl-or-Cmd,
`Ctrl`, `Cmd`/`Meta`, `Shift`, `Alt`/`Option`/`Opt`; last token is the key) for
dispatched entries, or a free-form display string (e.g. `← / →`, `1 – 9`) for
fixed reference entries. `dispatched:true` → matched live and needs a runtime
handler via `setHandler`; `dispatched:false` → handled by its own dedicated
handler elsewhere and listed for reference only. The two **mouse gestures** on
an instance — `moveInstance` (`Alt+Drag`) and `rotateInstance` (`Alt+Wheel`)
— are reference-only catalog entries so they are listed where people look for
shortcuts. So is
`nativeResolution` (`Shift+0`, View ▸ Native Resolution): matched BY PHYSICAL
KEY (`e.code === 'Digit0'`) in `ui/ui-wiring.js`'s transport handler beside
`+` / `-` / `0`, since Shift+0 types `)` on a US layout.

**Key exports.**
- `getDefaultTriangulationMethod()` / `setDefaultTriangulationMethod(method)` —
  read/write the default method used by implicit triangulation paths.
- `onDefaultTriangulationMethodChange(fn)` — called with the new method when
  `setDefaultTriangulationMethod` actually changes it (a throwing listener is
  ignored). `ui/ui-wiring.js` uses it to keep the toolbar Triangulate button's
  tooltip and its menu's Method radios in step with Settings.
- `getNodeWeight(name)` / `getNodeWeights()` / `getNodeWeightArray(nodeNames)` /
  `setNodeWeights(map)` — read/write per-node tracking weights (clamped to
  `[0,1]`; entries equal to the default `1` are dropped). `getNodeWeightArray`
  resolves a parallel weight array for an ordered node-name list — the form the
  tracker consumes. Writers: the Tracking Wizard (`setNodeWeights`, whole map),
  and via `updateNodeWeights` the first-use dialog (`ui/track-setup-modal.js`,
  via `pose/tracker.js`) and `ui/track-range-modal.js`.
- `updateNodeWeights(map)` — commit SOME nodes' weights, keeping every other
  stored override (`setNodeWeights(Object.assign(getNodeWeights(), map))`):
  weights are keyed by node name per browser, so another skeleton's must
  survive an edit to this one's.
- `getCameraWeight(name)` / `isCameraTracked(name)` / `getCameraWeights()` /
  `setCameraWeights(map)` — read/write per-camera tracking inclusion (coerced to
  `0|1`; entries equal to the default `1` are dropped so only excluded views
  persist). `isCameraTracked` is the boolean the tracker filters cameras by
  (`pose/tracker.js` `trackAll`/`trackCurrentFrame`); at least 2 views must stay
  included or tracking aborts with a warning.
- `updateTrackingThresholds(map)` — commit SOME thresholds, keeping every other
  stored override (the two tracking dialogs write the image-check settings
  through it).
- `getTrackingThresholdDefs()` / `getTrackingThreshold(id)` /
  `getTrackingThresholds()` / `setTrackingThresholds(map)` — read/write the
  tracker's user-editable thresholds. Three of them configure single-camera
  Track All (SLEAP's tracker, `pose/single-camera-tracking.js`): `scWindowSize`
  (sleap-nn `--tracking_window_size`, default 5), `scOksStddev` (`--oks_stddev`,
  default **0.1**, not sleap-nn's 0.025 — see that module) and `scConnectBreaks`
  (0/1, `--post_connect_single_breaks`, default 1), plus opt-in duplicate removal:
  `scRemoveDuplicates` (0/1, default 0), `scDuplicateIou` (0.5) and `scDuplicateOks`
  (0.6) — see `pose/single-camera-tracking.js` "Duplicate removal". `getTrackingThresholdDefs` returns the
  wizard's render catalog `[{ id, label, default, value, min, max, step, desc, kind }]`
  (`kind`: `'toggle'` for on/off settings, drawn as a switch; else `'number'`),
  **filtered to `WIZARD_THRESHOLD_IDS`** — the CrossViewTracker's free parameters
  only (`filterMinVisibleNodes`, `filterMinInstanceScore`, `filterMinMeanNodeScore`, `corr2dWeight`,
  `corr3dWeight`, `velocityThreshold`, `distanceThreshold`, `timePenalty`,
  `stale`, `matchGate` (0/1 toggle for the CrossViewTracker match gate),
  `maxPenalty` / `matchReproj` — the misplaced-node options, 0 (off) by
  default (the tracker's third, `birthMedian`, is not in the wizard),
  `scWindowSize`, `scOksStddev`, `scConnectBreaks`, `scRemoveDuplicates`,
  `scDuplicateIou`, `scDuplicateOks` (single camera),
  `reprojErrorThreshold`, `autoSwitchCheck` — 0/1, run the body-size ID-switch
  check after Track All / Track Frame Range, default 1; `autoImageSwitchCheck` —
  0/1, the image check likewise, default 0; `imageChangeThreshold` -0.8;
  `imageCheckHz` 2; `imageCheckBackgroundHz` 0.1 (crops per second away from contacts:
  `backgroundHz`, see pose/id-switch-check.js "Which samples are embedded"; Wizard only, not
  the Track All dialog); `imageCheckMaxViews` 3; `imageCheckWebNN` — 0/1, try WebNN,
  default 0; `autoBrightnessSwitchCheck` — 0/1, the coat-brightness check after
  SINGLE-CAMERA Track All, default 1; `brightnessCheckThreshold` -800;
  `brightnessCheckHz` 4). The remaining catalog entries (`epipolarDecay`, `reprojSigma`, `epipolarWeight`,
  `reprojWeight`, `minMatchScore`, `prevIdentityBonus`, `reprojGate2/3/4`,
  `track3dWeight`) drive the bench-only luc3d matcher and are hidden from the UI
  but still resolve via `getTrackingThreshold`. `getTrackingThresholds` returns
  the effective `{ id: value }` map the tracker snapshots per run; values clamp to
  range and entries equal to the default are dropped.
- `SMOOTHING_PARAMS` / `getSmoothingParamDefs()` / `getSmoothingParam(id)` /
  `getSmoothingParams()` / `setSmoothingParams(map)` — the Temporal Smoothing
  hyperparameters (luc3d #134): `smoothMedianWindow`, `smoothGaussianWindow`
  (both default **0 = off**, matching `reprojErrorThreshold`, so shipping the
  feature changes no existing project's 3D), `smoothMaxGap` (5),
  `smoothMinSegment` (5). A SEPARATE catalog from `TRACKING_THRESHOLDS`, not more
  entries in it: these are post-triangulation filter parameters rather than
  cross-view tracker knobs, and the Tracking Wizard's Thresholds section renders
  every threshold the catalog exposes. Note the `smooth*` prefix — `filter*` is
  already taken here for DETECTION filtering (`filterMinVisibleNodes`,
  `filterMinInstanceScore`). Same clamp + drop-if-default persistence as the
  thresholds, under `_settings.smoothingParams`. Read by `ui/ui-wiring.js` and
  passed to `smoothSession` (`pose/temporal-smoothing.js`).
- `getActions()` — catalog snapshot `[{ id, label, category, binding,
  defaultBinding, editable, dispatched }]` with effective bindings, for the modal.
- `getBinding(id)` — effective binding string (user override or catalog default).
- `setHandler(id, fn)` — attach the runtime handler for a dispatched action.
- `matchesBinding(id, e)` — true if a `KeyboardEvent` triggers the action under
  its effective binding (single-chord only; for external owners like
  `timeline-controller`).
- `dispatchEvent(e)` — resolve a `KeyboardEvent` to a dispatched action and run
  its handler (skipped when `shouldIgnoreShortcut(e)` says the key belongs to
  the focused control); returns `true` if handled. Supports
  **multi-key sequence** bindings (chords separated by spaces, e.g. `"g t"`) via a
  rolling keystroke buffer with a 1.2 s gap reset; single-chord bindings fire
  immediately, the longest matching sequence wins (ties → catalog order).
- `applyBindings(map)` — commit an `{ id: binding }` override map (editable-only;
  non-default, parseable chord/sequence strings; defaults dropped);
  `resetBindings()` clears all.
- `formatBinding(str)` — prettify a binding for display; renders the `Mod`
  token as **Cmd** on Apple devices and **Ctrl** elsewhere (via
  `navigator.platform`), so the Hot Keys modal and Settings panel show the
  device-appropriate modifier.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/keyboard-target.js` (the focus guard
`dispatchEvent` applies). That module imports nothing itself, so this one stays
bridgeable.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/settings-modal.js`,
`ui/track-range-modal.js`, `ui/id-switch-modal.js`, `ui/id-switch-highlight.js`,
`ui/rendering.js`, `ui/timeline.js`, `pose/tracker.js`, `pose/triangulation.js`,
`pose/triangulation-pool.js`.

---

### ui/detection-filter-preview.js

**Purpose.** The Tracking Wizard's readout, right under the three detection-
filter fields: what share of the active session's detections each filter
would remove at the values being typed — too few nodes, low instance score,
low mean node score — per camera and over the tracked cameras, with the share
the three remove together ("Removed") and the share failing more than one
("Fail 2+"). Below it, "How the detections are spread": per camera, a small
bar chart of each of the three quantities with its median, the bars below the
filter's minimum in orange.

**Key exports.**
- `buildDetectionFilterPreview({session, getValues, isCameraIncluded})` →
  `{root, start, update, dispose}`. `getValues()` returns `{minVisibleNodes,
  minInstanceScore, minMeanNodeScore}`. `start()` begins the one walk
  (`collectDetectionStatsAsync`, one camera per task) and is idempotent;
  `update()` re-counts from the recorded columns and re-renders;
  `dispose()` cancels a walk in flight.
- `fmtShare(part, whole)` — `12.3%`; `<0.1%` / `>99.9%` so a sliver is never
  rounded to 0% or 100%; `—` with nothing to divide by.

**Behavior.** Counts read `getValues()` (the wizard's unsaved `working`
values), so they move before Apply. A camera that loses far more than the
others (`flagOutlierCameras`) is flagged in each filter's own column, and — for
"Removed" — its row is coloured, tagged `loses far more`, and named in a
sentence under the table that also says what the total shows ("… The total
(2.4%) hides this."). A camera switched off in Camera Views is greyed, tagged
`excluded`, and left out of the total and of every comparison. Missing scores
are said, never shown as 0%: `no scores` / `no node scores` tags per camera, a
note for each (all-zero instance scores: a minimum above 0 removes every
prediction; no node scores: the node-score filter removes nothing), and the
chart says it instead of drawing bars. An empty session, no session, and a
failed walk each get a sentence instead of a table. Counts carry thousands
commas. One scroller: the block grows to its full height.

**Imports from project modules.** `../pose/detection-filter-stats.js`
(`collectDetectionStatsAsync`, `countDetectionFilters`, `detectionHistograms`,
`flagOutlierCameras`).

**Imported by.** `ui/settings-modal.js`.

**User-facing features.** Settings ▸ Tracking Wizard ▸ Tracking Thresholds:
"What these filters would remove". Covered by
`tests/e2e/detection-filter-preview.mjs` and
`tests/test-detection-filter-stats.mjs`.

---

### ui/settings-modal.js

**Resizable, draggable, and it remembers.** The card carries CSS `resize: both`
and is dragged by its header (minus the ×, via `noDragSelector`);
`installModalGeometry` (`ui/modal-geometry.js`, id `settings`) restores the last
size/position on open and records it on close. It is installed AFTER the
overlay is appended, because centring an un-remembered card needs its laid-out
size.

**The wizard's tables grow; they do not scroll.** Each of the three wizard
sections is a `<details>` built by `buildSection(title, count)`, rendering every
row; `.settings-panel-container` is the ONE scroller. Widening the modal reflows
the `auto-fill minmax(220px, 1fr)` grid; folding a section keeps the rest
reachable (no-scroll-within-scroll, CLAUDE.md). `<details>`/`<summary>` gives
keyboard and screen-reader support for free, and `ui/keyboard-target.js`
already knows a `SUMMARY` owns Space/Enter.

**Purpose.** Builds and shows the "Settings" modal (opened from Help ▸
Settings). Wizard-style layout: a left nav (`settings-nav`) of categories and a
right panel area (`settings-panel-container`), with a Cancel / Apply footer.

**Key exports.**
- `showSettingsModal(initialPanel)` — `initialPanel` ∈ `'triangulation'` |
  `'keyboard'` | `'wizard'` | `'smoothing'` (default `'triangulation'`).
  Single-instance.

**Behavior.** Four panels: **Default Triangulation** (the method is picked by
clicking one of two diagram cards from `ui/triangulation-methods-figure.js`,
DLT or "Refined (Ref)" = bundle adjustment — no separate rows; Enter/Space on a
focused card is handled in `onDocKeyDown`, which stops every keydown in the
capture phase, so a listener on the card would never run), **Keyboard Shortcuts** (the
`getActions()` catalog by category; an editable entry's key chip captures a
**chord or multi-key sequence** — keys accumulate, Ctrl/Cmd normalized to `Mod`
via `chordFromEvent`, until a click sets it or Esc cancels; duplicates are
rejected; fixed entries show a greyed dashed chip), **Tracking Wizard**:
**Node Weights** (number field `0–1` per node of the active skeleton; `0` drops
the node from the association cost and greys the row; under the hint, the
tracking dialogs' `NODE_WEIGHT_TIP` from `ui/track-setup-modal.js`), **Camera Views** (an
on/off switch per camera, stored as a `0/1` weight; off excludes the view from
tracking and greys the row), and **Tracking Thresholds** (one field per
`getTrackingThresholdDefs()` entry, or an on/off switch stored as `1`/`0` for a
`kind: 'toggle'` entry, via `makeToggle`). Covered by
`tests/e2e/settings-wizard-toggles.mjs`. And **Temporal Smoothing** (luc3d
#134 — one **Filter Parameters** section rendering `getSmoothingParamDefs()`:
median window, Gaussian window, max gap, minimum segment. Its own panel because
it is not a tracking parameter — it runs after triangulation, on 3D the tracker
has finished with. A note warns that raising the reprojection-error threshold
creates more gaps, hence more gap edges where the filter is least accurate. The
pass itself runs from the Triangulate / Triangulate All dropdowns.) Threshold
and smoothing rows share one `buildParamRow(def, bag, onEdit)` helper (same
`{ id, label, desc, min, max, step }` catalog shape, clamp on blur, `kind:
'toggle'` → switch; `onEdit` lets the filter fields re-count the preview).
Edits mutate a local `working` state only; nothing commits until **Apply**
(`setDefaultTriangulationMethod` + `applyBindings` + `setNodeWeights` +
`setCameraWeights` + `setTrackingThresholds` + `setSmoothingParams`), which
repaints overlays + timeline and re-fits the ID-switch box
(`refreshIdSwitchHighlight`, which encloses only nonzero-weight nodes). Cancel /
× / backdrop / Escape discard. Under the three detection-filter fields sits
`ui/detection-filter-preview.js`'s per-camera readout of what they would remove;
it re-counts on every `input` and camera switch (it reads `working`, so it moves
before Apply), and its one walk of the session starts the first time the wizard
panel is SHOWN (`selectPanel`), so Settings opened for anything else walks
nothing. Teardown disposes it. A capture-phase document keydown listener keeps
background shortcuts from firing while it is open, and is removed on teardown.

**Imports from project modules.** `./settings.js` (`getDefaultTriangulationMethod`,
`setDefaultTriangulationMethod`, `getActions`, `applyBindings`, `formatBinding`,
`getNodeWeight`, `setNodeWeights`, `getCameraWeight`, `setCameraWeights`,
`getTrackingThresholdDefs`, `setTrackingThresholds`, `getSmoothingParamDefs`,
`setSmoothingParams`); `./detection-filter-preview.js`
(`buildDetectionFilterPreview`); `./app-state.js`
(`getActiveSession`, `state`, `timeline`); `./rendering.js` (`drawAllOverlays`,
for the post-Apply repaint); `./modal-geometry.js` (`installModalGeometry`);
`./id-switch-highlight.js` (`refreshIdSwitchHighlight`, on Apply);
`./track-setup-modal.js` (`NODE_WEIGHT_TIP`, a leaf).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**User-facing features.** Settings modal — choose default triangulation method,
remap keyboard shortcuts, set per-node tracking weights, exclude camera views
from tracking (Tracking Wizard, also reachable via Tracks ▸ Tracking Wizard),
and tune the Temporal Smoothing filter (luc3d #134).

---

### ui/timeline.js

**Purpose.** SLEAP-like canvas timeline showing track occupancy bars,
frame markers, and current-frame indicator. Click-to-seek, drag-scrub,
shift-drag range select, mouse-wheel / pinch zoom, middle-click pan. It has
tree-grouped per-camera labels, an inner scrollable track-area
wrapper, and an empty-camera placeholder row per camera without tracks.
Rows whose camera is excluded from tracking in the Tracking Wizard
(`isCameraTracked(name)` false, imported from `./settings.js`) render grey —
both the gutter label and the occupancy bars — distinct from the
per-session visibility filter (`_hiddenCameras`), which drops the row entirely.

**The canvas is a BAND of the content, not all of it.** Inside the scroll
wrapper (`_trackScrollEl`), a spacer (`_spacerEl`) carries the timeline's full
content height — `max(getPreferredHeight(), visible height)` — so the scroll
range and native scrolling are what they always were. The canvas is absolutely
positioned over only a band of that content (`_placeBand`): one margin above
the scroll position to one margin below the visible bottom (margin = visible
height, at least 256 px), clamped to the content. Drawing stays in CONTENT
coordinates (the context transform carries `-_bandTop`); `_drawTrackBars` skips
rows outside the band. A scroll inside the band costs nothing; one that brings
the visible rows within half a margin of its edge (`_handleTrackScroll`) moves
the band and redraws. `_cssHeight` and `_layout` are still the CONTENT's; the
hover tooltip is placed from the pointer's client position (canvas `offsetY`
is band-relative). On a 561-row project a redraw went ~279 ms -> ~28 ms.
Covered by `tests/test-timeline-visible-band.js` and the backing-store clamp
cases in `tests/test-timeline-height.js`.

**Canvas backing-store cap.** `_placeBand` still clamps the backing store to
`MAX_CANVAS` (32000px/side): a `<canvas>` larger than ~32767px per side fails
to allocate ("sad face"). When it would be exceeded the effective
device-pixel ratio is scaled down (CSS size + scroll unchanged).

**The playhead is an element, not canvas paint.** `setCurrentFrame` moves a
positioned `div.timeline-playhead` (inside `_trackScrollEl`; `pointer-events:
none`) with a CSS transform — `_positionPlayhead` — and repaints NOTHING. The
canvas is redrawn only when what it shows changes: data, zoom, size, range
selection, or a scroll of the visible window (including one `setCurrentFrame`
makes following a frame out of a zoomed window). Painting it per step hit
Chrome's 2D-canvas GPU backpressure and capped held-arrow stepping (~19 vs 33
frames/s). `opts.playback` is still accepted and changes nothing.

`setFrameModified` asks for a repaint (`_requestRedraw`: coalesced to one
`requestAnimationFrame`, synchronous without rAF) when the flag actually
changes, since the white "modified" line is canvas paint. Covered by
`tests/test-timeline-playback-playhead.js`.

**Trackpad / wheel semantics.** `_handleWheel`: horizontal-dominant scroll
pans `_scrollFrame` (`preventDefault()` only when the pan moved);
**Shift+wheel** scrolls the track rows (`_trackScrollEl.scrollTop`); a **plain
wheel**, Ctrl/Cmd+wheel or trackpad pinch (`wheel` with `ctrlKey: true`) zooms
the time axis anchored on the frame under the cursor. `styles.css` defeats
macOS's overlay scrollbar on `.timeline-track-area::-webkit-scrollbar` so the
bar stays visible, and `scrollbar-gutter: stable` keeps the canvas width steady.

**Key exports.**
- `Timeline` — class. Selected methods: `setData(session)`,
  `setCurrentFrame(frameIdx, opts?)`, `setTotalFrames(n)`, `setZoom(level)`,
  `scrollTo(frameIdx)`, `resize`, `redraw`, `destroy`,
  `setDisplayMode(mode)`, `refreshTracks(session, opts?)`, `recolorTracks()`,
  `setFrameModified(frameIdx, modified)`, `getPreferredHeight`,
  `getCameraGroups`, `getLabelLines`, `getRowCount`,
  `getTrackAreaElement`.

**Track rows follow the Tracks / Identity coloring.** The constructor takes
`options.getColorByIdentity` (`pose/initialization.js` passes a reader of
`state.colorByIdentity`), and a track row's bar is `getTrackColor(trackIdx,
colorByIdentity)`, so the bar wears the palette the canvas draws that track in.
Rows whose color comes from a track index carry `_trackColored`;
`recolorTracks()` repaints just those and redraws. It does NOT rebuild:
segments do not depend on the mode, and `_buildTrackSegments` walks every frame
of every camera. The Tracks / Identity change handler (`ui/ui-wiring.js`) calls it.
Covered by `tests/e2e/unidentified-track-palette.mjs`.

**Possible-ID-switch markers** are not drawn here — they are on the
transport seekbar (`ui/seekbar-markers.js`).

**Initial-load 30% cap.** `setData(session)` sizes the container via
`_fitContainerToData()`, which clamps the container height to
`[preferred, floor(0.3 * window.innerHeight)]`: a small track set shows
fully (no forced empty space), while a set taller than 30% of the window
caps at 30% and the inner `_trackScrollEl` scrolls. `refreshTracks` stays grow-only so a height the
user expanded mid-session is never clipped.

**Segment draw clipping.** `_computeSegmentDrawRect()` draws wide segments
(`rawWidth >= minSegW`) at their true extents clipped to the visible rect; only
narrow segments get the min-width center-and-clamp treatment (clamping a wide
segment's midpoint made bars "fill in/out" while panning).

**`refreshTracks` modes.** Default: rebuild segments, `_growContainerToFit`
(grow-only), `resize()` — used by track add / rename / delete. `{ keepSize: true
}` skips both, so container height AND canvas pixel size stay as the user left
them — used by the visibility toggles in `ui/info-panel.js` (otherwise hiding
rows shrinks the canvas and pulls the playhead / labels up). `{ cap: true }`
re-applies the 30% cap (`_fitContainerToData`) — used after Track All / Track
Frame, Triangulate (current / all / group-by-identity), Propagate IDs↔Tracks
and multi-frame identity assignment, which can add many rows at once.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./overlays.js` — `getTrackColor`, `NULL_ID_COLOR`.
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `someValidPoint3d`, `points3dNodeCount`, `hasPoint3d`.
- `./settings.js` — `isCameraTracked`.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`.

**User-facing features.** Bottom timeline widget — seek, scrub, zoom,
range-select, modified-frame markers, per-track occupancy bars,
display mode toggle. Each camera renders as a tree-grouped block
(`┌─` / `├─` / `└─`) in the label gutter with the **camera name drawn
in bold** so it pops against the regular-weight track / identity names;
cameras with no tracks still occupy one placeholder row (`camName ──`).
When the natural row count exceeds the timeline container height, the
track area scrolls vertically while the mode-toggle / playhead chrome
stays fixed.

**Label gutter sizing.**
- `LEFT_MARGIN` is **dynamic** — recomputed each `_rebuildSegments` by
  `_recomputeLeftMargin()`. Per spec, the gutter is sized to the
  longest name **in the currently viewed tab** (`tracks` / `identities`
  / `both`); switching tabs may therefore resize the gutter to fit
  that tab's data. Clamped between `MIN_LEFT_MARGIN = 100` and
  `MAX_LEFT_MARGIN = 280`.
- Labels are drawn as **three columns** rather than one right-aligned
