  string. `_recomputeLeftMargin()` measures the three column widths
  separately and `_drawTrackBars` positions each piece at its own X:
  ```
  [LABEL_LEFT_PAD][ camName ][GAP][ connector ][trackName ]
                    bold,         left-align    left-align
                    right-align   at fixed X    at fixed X
  ```
  `_connectorForRole(role)` returns bracket glyphs of equal width
  (`┌─ ` / `├─ ` / `└─ ` / `── ` / `──`), so brackets line up within each
  camera group whatever the name lengths. The camera name is drawn only on
  the anchor row (`first` / `only` / `empty`); no `│` glyph is drawn.
- `_finalizeTreeGrouping()` does NOT call `_recomputeLeftMargin()` —
  `_rebuildSegments()` is the sole caller (after finalize).
- Composed `_trackNames` strings (`getLabelLines()`, used by tests) embed the
  camera name on anchor rows and a literal `│` on `middle` / `last` rows. They
  are **inspection-only**; the draw path uses `cameraName` / `trackName` /
  `treeRole` directly.

**Both-mode empty-camera dedupe.** In `'both'` display mode,
`_rebuildSegments` runs the tracks build and the identities build
sequentially, then merges by camera. For cameras with no tracks AND
no identities, both passes would emit a placeholder — the merge keeps
exactly one (`emptyEmittedForCam` flag) so the gutter doesn't show
the same empty camera twice.

**3D-points-only projects.** `_rebuildSegments` first checks
`_is3DPointsProject(session)` — true when the session has no cameras but its
`instanceGroups` carry `group.points3d` (skeleton + `handleLoadPoints3dH5`),
where the per-camera builders would produce zero rows. `_build3DPointsSegments`
builds one row per track/identity directly from the InstanceGroups
(occupancy = frames where the group has ≥1 non-null 3D keypoint), colored by
`getTrackColor(identityId)`, under a synthetic `'3D'` camera group so the
existing tree-grouping / draw / visibility paths work unchanged. Covered by
`tests/test-timeline-3dpoints.js`.

**Sparse occupancy + row cap (lazy `.slp`).** `_buildTrackSegments` reads
`session.trackOccupancy` two ways: a **dense** `{ data, nTracks, nFrames }` grid
(worker/analysis path) or **sparse** run-segments from a lazy `.slp`
(`SioLazyLoader._computeSparseOccupancy`: `{ sparse:true,
segments:Map<trackIdx,[{start,end}]>, counts }`), used directly with
materialized frames subtracted (`_subtractFramesFromSegments`) — for those
frames the live `fg.instances` win. Rows **cap** at `MAX_TRACK_ROWS_PER_CAMERA`
(32) per camera: the first-N tracks by **appearance** (earliest segment start),
in track-index order, plus a label-only `+N more` row; `counts` does not drive
the cap. Covered by `tests/test-timeline-sparse-occupancy.js`.

**ID Timeline and TRACKLESS ungrouped instances.** Pass 1 of
`_buildIdentitySegments` resolves each unlinked row with
`session.getIdentityIdForUnlinkedInstance(cam, instance, frameIdx)`, not the
track-keyed `getIdentityIdForTrack`. A trackless ungrouped instance keeps its
identity on the instance (`Instance.identityId`, stamped by `unlinkGroup` —
`frameIdentityMap` is keyed by trackIdx and a null track has no key), and
asking by track would read the shared per-camera "-1" slot instead. The
unlinked resolver delegates to the track-keyed one whenever a track exists.
Test: `tests/test-timeline-tree-grouping.js`. The same rule applies in
`ensureGroupsFromIdentities` (`pose/triangulation.js`),
`groupByIdentityAndTriangulateAll` (`ui/export-modals.js`) and
`_identityMatches` (`ui/custom-delete-ops.js`).

**ID Timeline population after lazy eviction.** `_buildIdentitySegments` (the
identity-mode counterpart to `_buildTrackSegments`) cannot rely on
`session.frameGroups` alone: on a lazy project it holds only resident frames.
Two passes: pass 1 scans `frameGroups` and is AUTHORITATIVE for the frames it
covers; pass 2 falls back to `session.frameIdentityEntries()` (the
`FrameIdentityMap`, `pose/pose-data.js`) for every other frame. No caching —
it re-runs on every `setDisplayMode`/`setData`/`refreshTracks`.
`frameIdentityMap` is the right fallback (not `instanceGroups` +
`trackOccupancy`): it covers the WHOLE tracked range, is never evicted, is
tiny, and its value IS the answer (`>= 0` → identityId, `< 0` → explicit
no-identity, which also gives the gray "No ID" row). Accepted, self-healing
tradeoff: `removeInstance`/`removeInstanceGroup` don't clean its entry, so an
unvisited frame may show a stale bar until visited. (`_parseFrameIdentityKey`
at the top of the file is no longer called here.) Covered by
`tests/test-timeline-tree-grouping.js`.

**Visibility panel row sizing.** `styles.css` scopes a compact 28×16
`.toggle-switch` to `.vis-toggle-row .toggle-switch`; `#visTimelineCameras` is
borderless tabular rows with no inner scrollbar, while Tracks and Identities
keep the scrollable `.vis-toggle-list`.

**Test fixture — flex layout.** `tests/test-runner.html` does not load
`styles.css`, so `tests/test-timeline-scroll.js`'s `createContainer()` mirrors
`.timeline-container`'s `display: flex; flex-direction: column` inline — without
it `_trackScrollEl`'s `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0` constrains nothing.

**Visibility filter.** The builders tag every row `_isTrack` or `_isIdentity`
(placeholders included). `_applyVisibilityFilter(session)` runs AFTER the
both-mode interleave and BEFORE `_finalizeTreeGrouping`, so the tree-role pass
sees the final rows. It inlines `ensureHiddenSets` (so `timeline.js` does not
import `timeline-visibility.js`) and returns early when all three Sets are
empty, leaving unfiltered output byte-identical. Per camera group:
1. Camera in `_hiddenCameras` → drop the whole group, header included (view
   precedence beats per-row toggles).
2. Else keep `treeRole === 'empty'` placeholders and drop rows whose
   `trackName` is in the hidden Set chosen by `_isTrack` / `_isIdentity`
   (un-flagged rows check `_hiddenTracks`).
3. A camera that had real rows but keeps none → one
   `{ treeRole: 'empty', isAllHidden: true, cameraName }` row, so its header
   survives.

`_finalizeTreeGrouping` copies `isAllHidden` onto `_cameraGroups[i]`;
`_drawTrackBars` draws such an anchor row's camera name in a dim
`rgba(255,255,255,0.25)` (restoring `fillStyle` after) — otherwise identical to
the no-data placeholder.

---

### ui/timeline-controller.js

**Purpose.** Timeline toggle/fit/shortcut controller. Encapsulates collapse/expand with prior-height cache, fit-to-data
sizing (capped at 30% of `window.innerHeight`), the toolbar-button
sync helper, and the Ctrl/Cmd+J (toggle) / Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+J ("Change
Frame Number") keyboard-shortcut installer. Has zero transitive
`app.js` imports so it can be bridged into the test runner.

**Key exports.**
- `toggleTimeline`, `collapseTimeline`, `fitTimelineToData`,
  `syncTimelineToggleButton`, `installTimelineShortcuts`,
  `getCachedTimelineHeight`, `setCachedTimelineHeight`.
- `collapseTimeline()` closes the timeline only if it is open (never opens
  it), via `toggleTimeline()` so the height cache and toolbar button match a
  manual collapse. Returns whether it collapsed anything.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./app-state.js` — `state` (for `state.timeline`).

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `pose/tracker.js`
(`collapseTimeline`, after Track All), `ui/ui-wiring.js`
(re-exports the same surface so legacy `import { toggleTimeline, … } from
'./ui-wiring.js'` keeps working).

**User-facing features.** Ctrl/Cmd+J toggles the timeline (remembering
its prior height); Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+J fires the legacy "Change Frame
Number" inline edit on the bottom-bar frame counter. When collapsed,
the timeline is **fully hidden** (`.collapsed` overrides the 40px
`min-height` with `height: 0 !important; min-height: 0 !important`); the
`.split-handle.horizontal` above it stays visible for drag-to-expand.

---

### ui/timeline-visibility.js

**Purpose.** Per-session Views / Tracks / Identities
visibility toggles for the timeline. Owns the toggle API, the source-of-truth
lists used by the **Info Panel → Visibility → Timeline** subsection, and the
membership queries that `ui/timeline.js`'s `_applyVisibilityFilter` reads at
build time. Module is stand-alone — **no imports** from other project modules,
so it loads cleanly in the headless node test runner without dragging in
`app.js`.

**Key exports.**
- `ensureHiddenSets(session)` — lazy-init `session._hiddenCameras`,
  `session._hiddenTracks`, `session._hiddenIdentities` as empty `Set`s.
  Idempotent; called at the top of every helper so callers never null-guard.
- `toggle{Camera,Track,Identity}Visibility(session, name)` — flip Set
  membership. Returns the new visible boolean.
- `is{Camera,Track,Identity}Visible(session, name)` — `true` if not hidden.
- `list{Cameras,Tracks,Identities}ForVisibility(session)` — `string[]`. The
  camera list is filtered by `session._uploadedCameras` (matching the
  timeline's own filter) so calibration-only cameras don't appear in the
  toggle list.
- `get{Camera,Track,Identity}VisibilityList(session)` — `[{ name, visible }]`
  (identity rows also include `id` and `color`). Track-row swatch color
  is intentionally NOT set here — `ui/info-panel.js` adds
  `getTrackColor(i, state.colorByIdentity)` — so this module stays free of
  `./overlays.js` and bridgeable without loading the app.
- `renameHiddenTrack(session, oldName, newName)` /
  `renameHiddenIdentity(session, oldName, newName)` — migrate hidden-set
  membership when the user renames a track / identity, so the toggle stays
  applied to the renamed entity.
- `serializeHiddenSets(session)` → a partial `metadata.lucid` fragment carrying
  only the NON-EMPTY sets (`hiddenCameras` / `hiddenTracks` /
  `hiddenIdentities`), or **`null`** when nothing is hidden. Names are sorted,
  so the written bytes depend on which entities are hidden and not on the order
  the user clicked them.
- `ingestHiddenSets(session, lucid)` — merge saved arrays back onto the Sets.
  Reads all three keys off ONE object, so a caller cannot wire up two of the
  three and silently drop the last. Additive, idempotent, and tolerant of a
  missing/garbage payload. Returns the count applied.

**Per-session state.** Lives directly on the `session` object as `Set<string>`
fields (keyed by entity NAME, including identities). Empty by default — fresh
sessions / new entities default to visible. Never cached in
localStorage (a browser-local cache would be wrong for what is project state),
but **persisted per session into the `.slp`** via the serialize/ingest pair
above — which is also why `ingestHiddenSets` does not validate names against
the session's current entities: a stale entry is harmless (it simply never
matches), exactly as it is after a delete.

**Global mirror.** The file also exposes the surface on
`window.TimelineVisibility.*` and individually (`window.toggleCameraVisibility`
etc.), guarded by `typeof window !== 'undefined'`, for the browser test runner.

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/info-panel.js` (toggle helpers + list helpers),
`ui/ui-wiring.js` (rename-migration helpers),
`import-export/visibility-metadata.js` (the serialize/ingest pair).
`ui/timeline.js` intentionally does **not** import this module — it inlines its
own `ensureHiddenSets` equivalent so the timeline core stays decoupled from the
visibility-panel wiring.

**User-facing features.** Backs the **Info Panel → Visibility → Timeline**
subsection. Hiding a camera hides every row for it; hiding all its
tracks/identities leaves a gray "all hidden" camera header.

---

### ui/track-identity-ops.js

**Purpose.** Pure, DOM-free operations backing the Tracks-menu New / Rename /
Delete modals (which live in `ui/ui-wiring.js`). Extracted so the substantive
logic is unit-testable headlessly — `ui/ui-wiring.js` itself can't be loaded in
the test runner (app.js import graph).

**Key exports.**
- `nameExists(session, kind, name)` — duplicate-name guard (`kind` =
  `'track' | 'identity'`).
- `countNulledByCamera(session, kind, idx)` → `{ perCamera, total }` — the
  Delete modal's per-camera breakdown of instances that will be nulled. Identity
  counting uses the **canonical per-frame identity source**
  (`session.getIdentityIdForTrack(cam, trackIdx, frameIdx)`), NOT
  `group.identityId` (which is only populated after triangulation).
- `deleteTrackAt(session, idx)` — first **ungroups** any GroupedInstance that
  uses the deleted track (`session.unlinkGroup`, members return to the unlinked
  pool); then splices the track, nulls every instance on it (`trackIdx = null`,
  the app-wide trackless sentinel — NOT -1, which crashes the overlay renderer),
  and shifts higher `trackIdx` down — over frameGroups (linked + unlinked) AND
  remaining GroupedInstances, with a `seen` set so shared refs aren't
  double-decremented. Remaps `frameIdentityMap` entries in lockstep
  (deleted-track entries move to the legacy `"frame:cam:null"` key, higher ones
  shift down) so an instance keeps its identity when it loses its track.
  Returns the name. On a lazy project it ALSO re-indexes the store's
  `instancesData.track` column (`session.lazyLoader.remapTracksFromIdentity`;
  deleted → -1, higher → down one); without it every non-resident instance
  above `idx` saves under the WRONG track name.
- `deleteIdentityAt(session, idx)` — **ungroups** every GroupedInstance carrying
  the id (matched via `group.identityId` OR, pre-triangulation, via the per-frame
  `getIdentityIdForTrack`; falls back to nulling `group.identityId` in sessions
  without `unlinkGroup`), clears the per-frame `frameIdentityMap` entries pointing
  at it (so instances resolve to "no identity"), splices the identity, and drops
  the hidden-identities entry. Returns the name.

**Imports from project modules.** None (operates on the passed `session`) — it
reaches the columnar store through the `session.lazyLoader` handle it is given.

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js`. Bridged into `tests/test-runner.html` and
covered by `tests/test-track-identity-modals.js`; the lazy/durable half of
`deleteTrackAt` is covered by `tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs` (cycle 5b).

---

### ui/track-range-modal.js

**Purpose.** The **Track Frame Range** dialog — the start/end picker behind the
Track Frame split button's dropdown, for re-running tracking over a narrow
window (e.g. around a known ID switch) while iterating on settings.

**Key exports.**
- `showTrackRangeModal(opts)` — builds the modal (standard
  `.multi-frame-modal-overlay` / `.multi-frame-modal` markup), validates
  inline, and on **Continue** calls `trackFrameRange(start, end)`
  (`pose/tracker.js`), which owns the loading overlay and the status line from
  there. Cancel, `Esc` and a backdrop click all close it with no side effects.
  `opts.onTracked(frameIdx)` is called with the last frame tracked on success.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/app-state.js` (`state`,
`getActiveSession`), `import-export/save-load.js` (`setStatus`),
`pose/tracker.js` (`trackFrameRange`, `trackableFrameBounds`,
`getTrackerNumAnimals`, `setTrackerNumAnimals`), `pose/placeholder-cameras.js`
(`hasRealCalibration` — with placeholder cameras only, the dialog does not open
and the status says tracking needs a calibration), `ui/settings.js`
(`getNodeWeight`, `updateNodeWeights`, `getTrackingThresholdDefs`,
`updateTrackingThresholds`), `ui/track-setup-modal.js`
(`buildNodeWeightFields`, `buildImageCheckFields`, `IMAGE_CHECK_IDS`; a leaf).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (wires `#tbTrackFrameRange`, the dropdown
item; the `#tbTrackFrame` button itself stays wired to `trackCurrentFrame` in
`pose/tracker.js`'s own IIFE). It sits between `ui-wiring.js` and `tracker.js`,
which `ui-wiring.js` already imported directly — so it introduces no new cycle.

**User-facing features.** A **dual range slider** over the session extent
(`.range-slider-container` markup), two-way bound to Start (pre-filled with the
current frame) and End (a 100-frame window, clamped); **Animals** (blank =
auto-detect); the skeleton's **node weights** (`buildNodeWeightFields`) and the
**image ID-switch check** section (`buildImageCheckFields`), both written on
Continue (`updateNodeWeights` / `updateTrackingThresholds`) before the run
reads them — Cancel / Esc write nothing; a weight outside `[0, 1]` disables
Continue. The card is 620px wide and the one scroller, with a sticky button
row. A live summary line gives range size and animal count; a live error line
explains an invalid range and disables Continue. `Enter` = Continue, `Esc` =
Cancel. On success the viewer is parked on the **last frame tracked**.

**Notes / caveats.**
- **It collects the animal count on purpose.** Otherwise `pose/tracker.js`'s
  first-use dialog would pop up ON TOP of this one; `runTrackingPass` skips it
  on the range path, so a blank Animals field is an explicit "auto-detect" —
  and it is written through on every Continue, so clearing it really turns
  auto-detect back on.
- **The range is tracked in isolation** (no history from before its first
  frame), as the modal's body text says — identities need not line up with the
  frames around it. See `runTrackingPass`.
- **Frame numbers are 0-based inside, 1-based on screen.** `toDisplay` /
  `toIndex` hold both conversions and `readForm` is the single crossing point:
  number inputs and endpoint labels carry DISPLAY values, range sliders and
  `bounds` carry indices. `pose/tracker.js` has its own `displayFrame()`.
- **The slider spans `[bounds.min, bounds.max]`, not `[0, max]`** — a sparse
  non-lazy project starts at its first labelled frame, and `updateSliderFill`
  takes percentages across that real extent.
- **Neither thumb may pass or drag the other** — each STOPS at the other
  (pushing would silently move an endpoint already set). Typed inverted input
  is instead reported ("The end frame must not be before the start frame.") and
  disables Continue, never rewritten.
- **`onTracked` is injected, not imported.** `navigateToFrame` lives in
  `pose/initialization.js`, which imports `ui/ui-wiring.js`, which imports this
  module — importing it here would close that loop. It parks on the range
  `trackFrameRange` REPORTS (clamped, normalized), not the typed values.
- **`--accent-color` must stay defined.** `:root` aliases
  `--accent-color: var(--accent)`; without it `.range-slider-fill` and every
  `.multi-frame-modal button.primary` resolve to `transparent`.
- Covered by `tests/e2e/track-frame-range.mjs` (phases 4–5).

---

### ui/track-setup-modal.js

**Purpose.** What Track All and Track Frame ask the first time they run on a
session: the **number of animals**, with the skeleton's **node weights** laid
out under it for review. Node weights reach the tracker's association cost
(`pose/cross-view-tracker.js`) but are saved per BROWSER, keyed by node name,
so a weight left at 0 from another project silently drops a node from this
one's matching; this dialog is the one moment every user passes before
tracking a session.

Track All's copy also carries the **image ID-switch check**: its switch
(`autoImageSwitchCheck`, off by default) and, while on, its four options — the
Tracking Wizard's own settings. Track Frame's copy leaves it out (a single
frame is never checked); Track Frame Range shows the same section.

**Key exports.**
- `NODE_WEIGHT_TIP` — the advice shown under the node weights here, in Track
  Frame Range and in the Tracking Wizard (nodes with high tracking error, like
  tail nodes, weighted 0 often prevent ID switches; consider 0 for every tail
  node). Advice only: skeletons differ between projects, so
  nothing picks the nodes or changes a weight for the user.
- `buildNodeWeightFields(nodes)` → `{element, read, refresh}` — the node-weight
  editor both tracking dialogs show (title, one sentence, the tip, the grid). `element`
  is null for no nodes; `read()` returns `{nodeWeights}` or `{error}` (a weight
  outside `[0, 1]`, naming the node — never clamped); `refresh()` greys the
  rows weighted 0. Commits nothing. Also used by `ui/track-range-modal.js`.
- `showTrackSetupModal({title, animals, nodes, imageCheck})` →
  `Promise<{animals, nodeWeights, thresholds} | null>`. `title` names the run
  ("Track All" / "Track Frame"); `animals` pre-fills the count (null = blank =
  auto-detect); `nodes` is `[{name, weight}]` in skeleton order with each
  node's CURRENT effective weight; `imageCheck`, when given, is the image
  check's `getTrackingThresholdDefs()` entries. Resolves
  `{animals: number|null, nodeWeights: {name: w}, thresholds: {id: v}}` (every
  node and setting shown, edited or not; `{}` thresholds when none were) on
  Continue / Enter, and `null` on Cancel / Esc / a backdrop click. A second
  call while one is open resolves `null` at once.
- `IMAGE_CHECK_IDS` — the image check's settings in dialog order, the switch
  first.
- `buildImageCheckFields(defs)` → `{element, read, refresh}` — the image-check
  section: the switch, a one-line caution, and the options in the weight
  grid's compact markup, hidden while the switch is off. Labels are the
  Wizard's less its "Image check: " prefix; the Wizard's long descriptions sit
  in the labels' `title`. `read()` refuses a value outside its setting's range,
  never clamps it. Commits nothing. `buildImageCheckFields(defs, after)` words
  the switch "…after `after`" (default "Track All"); Track Frame Range passes
  "tracking the range".

**Imports from project modules.** None — a LEAF on purpose. It commits
nothing: `pose/tracker.js` (`askTrackSetup`) writes the count through
`setTrackerNumAnimals`, the weights through `updateNodeWeights` and the image
check through `updateTrackingThresholds` — the settings the toolbar's Animals
field and the Tracking Wizard edit, so there is no second copy of any of them
and no cycle with the tracker.

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js`; `ui/track-range-modal.js`
(`buildNodeWeightFields`, `buildImageCheckFields`, `IMAGE_CHECK_IDS`);
`ui/settings-modal.js` (`NODE_WEIGHT_TIP`).

**User-facing features.** The count field (focused, `placeholder="auto"`),
then one row per skeleton node in the Tracking Wizard's own markup
(`.settings-node-weight-row`, `.settings-num-input`; a weight of 0 greys its
row, as there), then where to change both later — naming only what it shows,
since single-camera Track All passes no nodes and gets no weight grid. Enter is Continue and Esc is
Cancel, consumed in the capture phase so no app shortcut also sees them.

**Notes / caveats.**
- **Bad input is refused, never clamped.** A count that is not a whole number
  ≥ 1 (or text the field reports as `''`, `validity.badInput`) and a weight
  outside `[0, 1]` show an inline error naming the node and disable Continue —
  a typed 5 is a typo, not a request for 1. The Wizard clamps on blur instead.
- **One scroller.** The card caps its height and scrolls; the weight grid never
  does. Its button row is `position: sticky` (as in Track Frame Range and the
  Track All summary). The grid is `minmax(160px, 1fr)`, narrower than the
  Wizard's.
- **Tests answer it with `tests/e2e/fixtures/track-setup.js`**
  (`answerTrackSetup(page, animals)`); `tests/tracker-gui-hooks.mjs` stubs
  it for the Node GUI test.

Covered by `tests/e2e/track-setup-modal.mjs` and `tests/test-tracker-gui.mjs`.

### ui/trail-presets.js

**Purpose.** Node-trail lengths, in SECONDS — the presets Off, ¼ s, ½ s, 1 s, 2 s, and
any custom length typed into Tracks ▸ Node Trails ▸ Custom…, in seconds or in
frames — and their
conversion to the frames a trail draws (a fixed frame count would mean a
different duration on every camera). DOM-free and import-free, so
`ui/app-state.js` can import it and `tests/test-trail-presets.mjs` runs it in Node.

**Key exports.**
- `TRAIL_PRESETS` — `{key, seconds, name}` per preset; `key` names the Tracks ▸
  Node Trails item ids (`menuTrails<key>`).
- `trailFrames(seconds, fps)` — `round(seconds × fps)`, at least 1 for a trail
  that is on, at most `MAX_TRAIL_FRAMES`; 0 when off.
- `trailRate(fps)` — `fps`, or 30 while none is known (`state.fps` is 0 before a
  video loads), so a trail is never silently 0 frames.
- `trailPresetFor(seconds)` — the preset of exactly that length, or null (custom).
- `trailSecondsName(seconds)` — the preset's name, or a custom length in seconds
  to at most 3 decimals ("1.5 seconds").
- `trailLabel(seconds, fps)` — "½ second (30 frames)", "1.5 seconds (90
  frames)", or "Off". The menus, the button's tooltip and the status line all
  use it.
- `parseTrailSeconds(text)` — the Custom… Seconds field: a plain decimal above 0
  (a decimal comma is accepted, since the field is text), else null.
- `parseTrailFrames(text)` — the Custom… Frames field: a whole number of at
  least 1, else null.
- `formatTrailSeconds(seconds)` — how the Seconds field shows a length: at most
  3 decimals, no trailing zeros ("0.167").
- `trailSecondsForFrames(frames, fps)` — how a length typed in frames is stored:
  `frames / fps`, EXACTLY (10 frames at 60 fps is 1/6 s, not 0.167), so it draws
  the frames typed.
- `MAX_TRAIL_FRAMES` (500) — `LAZY_KEEP_BEHIND` (512) must stay above the longest
  trail, since trails draw resident frames only. The test asserts the inequality.
- `setTrailSeconds(state, seconds)` / `onTrailSecondsChange(fn)` — THE setter for
  `state.trailSeconds`, the `ui/color-by.js` pattern: it sets the state and, only
  on a change (it returns whether there was one), runs the ONE handler ui-wiring
  registers — both menus' checkmarks, the Trails button's blue and tooltip, a
  redraw. The menus go through it, and so does Track All (`pose/tracker.js`),
  which cannot import ui-wiring. A throwing handler is logged, never re-raised.
- `TRACK_ALL_TRAIL_SECONDS` (0.25) — the length Track All turns trails on at;
  a preset, so the menus check "¼ second" rather than "Custom".

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** `ui/app-state.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `pose/tracker.js`.

### ui/track-summary.js

**Purpose.** What the Track All summary box says, DOM-free so the decision tree is
unit-tested in Node (`tests/test-track-summary.mjs`). It gives every outcome
(switches found, nothing flagged, check not run) a visible next step.

**Key exports.**
- `summarizeTrackedIdentities(session, {frames, animals, animalsAuto, elapsedMs, fps,
  singleCamera, untracked, filtered, duplicates, reviewPlaces})`
  — frames per identity, frames with any identity, frames with every animal, from
  `session.instanceGroups` (never evicted on a lazy project, so the whole project).
  An identity listed twice in one frame counts once. A single-camera Track All
  passes its camera and the detections it left trackless, and is counted on the
  stand-in session (`pose/single-camera-tracking.js` `singleCameraCheckSession`).
- `describeSwitchCheck(enabled, res, identities, whyNotRun)` — one check's outcome
  as `{state, switches, encounters, moments, reason, elapsedMs}` (`moments`: the
  candidate moments coat brightness tested on one camera), state one of `found` /
  `clear` / `skipped` / `failed` / `cancelled` / `off` / `na` (one identity).
  Switches are `idSwitchOnsets` — the "possible switches" count the tab uses.
- `planTrackSummary(summary, checks)` — the rows, the notes, the next step and the
  buttons. Recommends **Review switches** when any check found one; **Triangulate
  All** otherwise (with "Check by images…" / "Check by body size" offered when that
  check did not run, and a caveat that body size cannot separate similar-sized
  animals when only size came back clear); **Tracking Wizard…** when nothing was
  matched. A skipped check never gets a "nothing found" headline. Notes flag more
  (or fewer) identities than animals.
  **A single-camera summary** (`summary.singleCamera`) is planned apart, so the
  multi-camera one is unchanged.
  - **What it reports:** it counts **Tracks**, since SLEAP's tracker writes one per
    animal, and adds a "Without a track" row for detections over the animal count
    plus those the Tracking Wizard's detection filters removed (`filtered`) and the
    duplicates removed first, each named apart, and a "To review" row for the places on its
    review list (`reviewPlaces`, with their rate a minute).
  - **Its checks:** Coat brightness (the automatic one, counting its candidate
    moments), Images, and Body size. Body size is "Not run" on one camera, with
    the reason on hover.
  - **Next step:** **Review switches** when one was flagged (with the review list's
    places named in the step); else **Review the list** when it has places ("Review
    the N places most likely to hold a switch"); otherwise **Save project** (the
    tracks are what SLEAP reads back). Each check that did not run is offered:
    images, coat brightness, and body size (optional on one camera). It never
    suggests Triangulate All: there is no 3D.
  - **"Nothing flagged", with no review list, carries a caveat** that the checks miss many swaps on one
    camera. On 87 proofread SLAP views the image check found 82 of 276, and 113
    of those 276 were one track changing animal while the other was undetected,
    so no contact was there to test.
- `formatShare(count, total)` — floored to one decimal, so only a full count reads
  "100%". `formatDuration(ms)` — "0.4 s", "42 s", "3 min 5 s", "1 h 12 min".
- `formatTrackingSpeed(frames, ms, recordingFps)` — the Tracking time row's detail:
  frames per wall-clock second ("300 fps") and, when the recording's rate is
  known, the multiple of real time ("10×") — that throughput over the recording
  fps, so the two cannot disagree.
- `MAX_IDENTITY_ROWS` (12) — identity rows listed before "and N more" (the modal
  gets no inner scroller). `CLEAR_NOTE` — a clear row's hover text.

**Notes / caveats.**
- **A clear check says "Nothing flagged in N close encounters", never "No possible
  switches".** Raising nothing does not show there is no switch (same-size
  animals, look-alikes, a single contact with no "before"), so the row states
  what the check did, in ordinary text colour rather than success green, and
  its tooltip (`CLEAR_NOTE`) says it can still miss one. The status line
  (`runIdSwitchChecks`) and the ID Switches tab's header use the same wording;
  a non-zero count still reads "possible switches".
- **A time is shown only for a check that spent real time** (found / clear /
  failed / cancelled); a skip returns at its preflight.
- **Long reasons are cut at their first parenthesis** (the skeleton skip lists 16
  bone pairs); the row carries the whole reason as `full`, which the modal puts in
  the row's tooltip.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/id-switch-review.js` (`idSwitchOnsets`,
`idSwitchEncounterCount`), itself import-free.

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js`, `ui/track-summary-modal.js`.

### ui/track-summary-modal.js

**Purpose.** The box that opens when Track All finishes: identities, frames
tracked, frames with every animal, tracking time with its speed (fps, × real
time), a bar per identity, the **Identities** section (`ui/identity-gallery.js` —
five sample images and a path map per identity), each
ID-switch check's result and time, and the **Next step** with its button focused.
Renders `planTrackSummary` (`ui/track-summary.js`) and the gallery, and nothing else.

**Key exports.** `showTrackSummaryModal(summary, checks, {session})` — returns
`{close, overlay, gallery}`, or null without a DOM (Node harnesses run Track All);
with `session` it adds the Identities section, whose `gallery.done` settles once
its images are drawn (`pose/tracker.js` and `reopenTrackSummary` pass it);
`setTrackSummaryNavigator(fn)` (ui-wiring registers `navigateToFrame`, as it does
for the ID Switches tab: a sample or a map point clicked closes the box and goes
to that frame — the tracker that opens the box cannot import `pose/initialization.js`);
`rememberTrackSummary(session, opts, checks, identities)` (Track All keeps what the
box showed on `session._trackSummary` — in memory, not saved — and refreshes the
ID Switches tab so its button appears); `reopenTrackSummary(session, latest?)` (the
tab's **Show Track All summary**, and the end of a check started from the box —
`latest` is that run's results); `summarySession(session)` (what the box counts and
draws from: the session itself, or for a single-camera one its stand-in from
`pose/single-camera-tracking.js` `singleCameraCheckSession(session, {pixelCamera:
true})` — one group per identified detection, and a PIXEL camera whose `project`
maps (x, y, 0) to (x, y), so the gallery's crops and path maps work unchanged; the
session's own stand-in camera would divide by z = 0. Null on a lazy project).

**One camera.** Single-camera Track All (`pose/tracker.js`
`runSingleCameraTrackAll`) opens the same box, planned for one camera
(`planTrackSummary` with `singleCamera`): tracks instead of identities, the
coat-brightness / image / size check rows, and review or **Save project**
(`#menuSaveProject`) as the next step. Reopening it re-counts on `summarySession`
and re-reads every check the pass reported, coat brightness included. The
Identities section has a row per track, cut from the one view.

**Reopening.** The ID Switches tab's button reopens the box
(`setTrackSummaryOpener`, registered at module load — this module imports
`ui/id-switch-modal.js`, so that one stays a leaf). Counts are re-taken from the
session as it is now; the tracking time is the pass's own. Each check shows its
CURRENT result (a check run since replaces "Not run" / "Skipped"; cleared
results read "Not run" again); a skip or failure is kept from the pass, because
`runIdSwitchChecks` stores only results that ran.

**A check run from the box comes back to it.** **Check by images…** / **Check by
body size** go through `clickCheckFromSummary`; when the check ends the box
reopens with that check's row filled in and the next step that follows (the tab
is not opened behind it). A cancel, skip or failure is written into the kept
summary through `reopenTrackSummary`'s `latest`, so the row reads e.g.
"Cancelled" (and still offers the check) here and on a later reopen.

**Notes / caveats.**
- **A note, not a prompt.** Nothing changes until a button is pressed; Close,
  `Esc` and a backdrop click dismiss it. Each action button clicks the existing
  control — `#tbTriangulateAll`, `#menuCheckImageSwitches`,
  `#menuCheckSizeSwitches`, `#menuCheckBrightnessSwitches` (all three through
  `clickCheckFromSummary`, so the box comes back when the check ends),
  `#menuSaveProject`, `#menuTrackingWizard` — so it runs exactly what the
  user would have run by hand (a disabled button, e.g. under Defining Plane Mode,
  ignores it the same way). **Review switches** opens the ID Switches tab and
  presses `#idSwitchNext`, landing on the first flagged switch.
- **Keys stop at it** (capture-phase listener, as `openFixDialog` does), so the
  app's shortcuts do not act under it; `Enter` / `Space` still press the focused
  primary button.
- **One at a time.** A second Track All closes the first box before opening its
  own.
- **No inner scroller.** The identity list is capped instead; the modal itself
  caps its height at the viewport. It drops `.multi-frame-modal`'s 420px
  `min-width` so it fits a narrow window. With the Identities section
  (`.has-gallery`) it widens to 780px and its button row is STICKY at the
  bottom, so the recommended step stays in view while the rows scroll.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/track-summary.js` (`planTrackSummary`,
`summarizeTrackedIdentities`, `describeSwitchCheck`), `ui/id-switch-modal.js`
(`openIdSwitchPanel`, `refreshIdSwitchPanel`, `setTrackSummaryOpener`,
`clickCheckFromSummary`), `ui/identity-gallery.js` (`renderIdentityGallery`).

**Imported by.** `pose/tracker.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`setTrackSummaryNavigator`).
No cycle: none of its imports reaches back to the tracker.

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/track-all-summary.mjs` (§2b reopening from the tab,
§2c checks run from the box, §8 a switch found by images); the Identities
section: `tests/e2e/identity-gallery.mjs`. Tests that click the app after a
Track All close the box first (`#trackSummaryClose`).

### ui/identity-gallery.js

**Purpose.** The Track All summary's **Identities** section: one row per identity
— its name, five sample images of that animal spread over the recording, each
from the view that shows it best and rotating through the views, and a map of its
path over one camera's view (the camera picked above the rows). An identity that
switched between animals that look different shows up as a row whose images are
not all the same animal, or a path that jumps across the cage — seen before
either ID-switch check is run.

**How a row is chosen** (the planning half, DOM-free):
- `identityTracks(session)` — every identity's tracked frames and 3D centroids,
  from `session.instanceGroups` (resident in full even on a lazy project). A
  frame where an identity has two groups is left out, as the image check does.
- `pickSampleFrames` — the middles of five equal slots of the identity's tracked
  span, each moved, when it has to be, to a frame where the animal is ISOLATED
  (its nearest other identity more than 1.5 of its body sizes away), the
  isolated frame nearest the middle winning (maximising isolation pushed picks
  to slot edges). Only the middle 60% of each slot is searched, so neighbouring
  samples stay 40% of a slot apart even in a crowded cage. Only frames where the
  animal has at least half its keypoints count (`keypointShare`): a stray
  detection — one keypoint wearing the identity for a frame between its real
  stretches — made an empty "no keypoints" picture (seen on a real sham_gdx
  video). A slot with none in its middle takes the usable frame nearest that
  middle, on either side; only an identity with no usable frame at all falls back
  to whatever is tracked.
- `pickCamera` — among the views where the group has a 2D member: the largest
  projection of its 3D (diagonal, with the camera's distortion) inside the image
  is the best, and among the views good enough to use (inside the image, at
  least 30% of the best's size and 40 px) the one this identity's samples used
  LEAST wins, so the five rotate through the views. A view whose 2D detection is
  poor (under half its keypoints, or spread 2.5x wider than the 3D) is passed
  over while any other is not: scattered keypoints cut a huge crop with a speck
  of animal in it.
- `cropSquare` — full colour, upright, unmasked: a square of 1.4x the keypoints'
  larger extent + 12 px. NOT the image check's masked, rotated greyscale crop,
  which is the model's input, not something a person compares coats in. The
  animal's own keypoints are drawn as small dots in its colour, which is what
  says which animal the row means when two share an image.
- `memberPointsByCamera` — the identity's 2D in every camera at one frame, read
  with `hydrateFrameMembers2d` / `releaseFrameMembers2d` around it: on a lazy
  project a member of a frame that is not resident holds only the shared
  placeholder (see CLAUDE.md "Lazy projects: resident frames are a window").
- `projectTrack` — the path in a camera's pixels, thinned evenly to
  `MAP_MAX_POINTS`, broken where frames are more than 2 s apart (the gap test
  scales with the thinning) or a point does not project; `defaultMapCamera` — the
  camera with the most 2D members over a thinned sample of the groups.

**Drawing.** `renderIdentityGallery(host, session, {onPick})` builds the rows
with placeholders at once and fills them AFTER the box paints: the maps first,
then each row's images, one decoded frame per sample (`view.decoder.getFrame`,
drawn immediately — a decoder's cached frame may be closed by the next decode).
It stops as soon as the box is closed (a few seconds on an 8–10-camera
session). Without a video a sample says "no video" and the maps draw on a dark
ground. The map is a darkened still of the chosen camera with the path coloured
by TIME on a one-hue sequential ramp and a 0:00 → end key; hovering shows the
nearest point's time, a click goes there. The chosen view is kept on
`session._galleryView` (in memory). Rows cap at `GALLERY_MAX_ROWS` (12).

**Hover to enlarge.** A sample shows at `GALLERY_ZOOM` (320 px) beside the
tile, inside the window, captioned with identity, time and camera.
- **The preview is a second copy of the crop, cut when the tile is drawn** (up
  to 320 px × devicePixelRatio, never more than the crop's video pixels).
  Re-decoding on hover is not possible: the decoder's frame may be closed, and
  a seek per hover is slow.
- **It lives in the summary's overlay, not the modal**, which scrolls and would
  clip it; closing the box removes it. It takes no pointer events, scrolling
  hides it, and a drawn sample drops its native `title` (which would surface
  over the preview). A sample with no image shows none.

**A map enlarges too**, at the view's shape (`GALLERY_ZOOM` tall, or
`MAP_ZOOM_MAX_WIDTH` (640 px) wide when narrower), in the same preview box. The
frame is copied once per map draw at that size (same closed-frame reason) and
the path redrawn over it on first hover with `drawPath`, shared with the small
map so the two cannot disagree. The point under the pointer is ringed and its
time captioned. Changing the map's camera hides a stale preview. The preview
reads the element's `_zoom` (`{kind, w, h, draw, color, label, frame}`): one box
serves both kinds.

**Key exports.** `renderIdentityGallery`, `identityTracks`, `identityGroupAt`,
`groupCentroid`, `pickSampleFrames`, `projectedBox`, `pickCamera`, `cropSquare`,
`memberPointsByCamera`, `projectTrack`, `defaultMapCamera`, `timeColor`;
`GALLERY_SAMPLES` (5), `GALLERY_MAX_ROWS`, `GALLERY_TILE` (88 px),
`GALLERY_ZOOM` (320 px), `MAP_ZOOM_MAX_WIDTH` (640 px), `MAP_MAX_POINTS` (2,400).

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/app-state.js` (`state`),
`pose/lazy-residency.js` (`hydrateFrameMembers2d`, `releaseFrameMembers2d`).

**Imported by.** `ui/track-summary-modal.js`.

**Coverage.** `tests/test-identity-gallery.mjs` (the planning half, including a
released lazy member read through hydration with the direct-read control) and
`tests/e2e/identity-gallery.mjs` (stand-in videos painting each animal its own
colour; negative control: identities swapped halfway, each row then showing
BOTH animals).

### ui/view-align-modal.js

**Purpose.** The **Align Views to References** dialog, opened from View ▸
"Align Views to References…" (`#menuAlignViews`). The user ticks two or more
views they have already rotated (Visibility ▸ Video Rotation or the Shift+R+←/→
chord); every other view is rotated to match, from the calibration
(`pose/view-align.js`).

**Key exports.** `showAlignViewsModal()`.

**Behaviour.**
- A table of the session's calibrated cameras: reference checkbox, current
  angle, and a live preview of the angle each will get (or why it is left
  unchanged). Inline errors (fewer than two references, an upside-down
  reference) disable Apply. Pre-selects the last references used, else the
  views already turned when there are at least two. `Esc`, Cancel and a backdrop
  click close with no change; `Enter` applies.
- **Writes both rotation copies**, as every rotation edit does: the session
  store (`setSessionRotation`, persisted in `metadata.lucid.videoRotation`) and,
  for the active session, `view.rotation` + `applyZoom` + `syncRotationUI`. Then
  one `drawAllOverlays` and `markDirty`. Angles are stored as whole degrees.
- **"Also apply to the other N sessions"** re-solves each other session from
  its OWN calibration with this session's reference angles; sessions missing a
  reference camera are listed in the status line, never half-applied. Edited
  background sessions get `isDirty = true` directly — `markDirty()` only flags
  the active one, and the switch-away prompt and lazy eviction read each
  session's own flag.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/app-state.js` (`state`,
`videoController`, `getActiveSession`), `import-export/save-load.js`
(`setStatus`, `markDirty`), `ui/rendering.js` (`drawAllOverlays`),
`ui/sessions-panes.js` (`syncRotationUI`), `ui/video-filters.js`
(`getSessionRotation`, `setSessionRotation`, `clampRotationSetting`),
`pose/view-align.js` (`alignViewRotations`).

**Imported by.** `ui/ui-wiring.js` (the View-menu click handler).

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/align-views-to-references.mjs`.

---

### ui/view-legend.js

**Purpose.** The Visibility panel's **Display Legend** key, as DOM chrome in
each video pane rather than pixels painted onto the overlay canvas.

It is appended to the `.video-cell` as a SIBLING of `.canvas-wrapper` (the
element `applyZoom` rotates), never inside it: painted into the canvas it would
rotate with the video, sit inside the picture of a letterboxed pane, and differ
in screen size between camera resolutions. Nothing outside the wrapper can be
reached by a view transform.

**Key exports.**
- `syncViewLegends(showLegend, opts)` — brings every view's pane into line with
  the current visibility state. Called from `drawAllOverlays`, so it inherits
  the overlays' triggers. Rebuilds a pane's rows only when the row SET changed
  (`dataset.legendKey`), since it runs on every playback frame.
- `legendItems(opts)` — the rows, in order. Mirrors `drawLegend`'s item list
  exactly (same rows, same order, same conditions) so the live legend and the
  exported one cannot disagree.

Swatches are inline SVG built from the same `TRACK_COLORS` / `REPROJECTION_COLOR`
constants the overlays draw with, so the key cannot drift from what it keys.
`pointer-events: none` is load-bearing — the overlay canvas underneath is the
click target for annotation, and the legend sits over its top-right corner.

**`drawLegend` is kept.** The overlay-video export burns the legend into
encoded frames, where there is no DOM (`drawTileContent`,
`ui/overlay-export-modal.js`, also outside the view transform).

**Styling.** `.view-legend` / `.view-legend-row` / `.view-legend-icon` in
`styles.css`, anchored `top:8px; right:8px` on the `.video-cell`. Three things
want that corner: the legend, the `.unzoom-btn` (only while zoomed) and
`#viewModeIndicator` (only in single-view mode, and on `#videoDock` rather than
the pane). Each of the latter two pushes the legend down one slot and both
together push it two, via `.video-cell.zoomed` and the
`#videoDock.has-view-indicator` class that `showViewIndicator` toggles.

**Imports.** `ui/app-state.js` (`state`), `ui/overlays.js` (the two color
constants).

**Imported by.** `ui/rendering.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/view-legend-pane-chrome.mjs`.

---

### ui/video-filters.js

**Purpose.** Per-CAMERA video display settings — brightness, contrast and
rotation. Pure and DOM-free: it imports NO project modules, so it can
be bridged into `tests/test-runner.html` without dragging `app.js` in.
`ui/sessions-panes.js` owns the DOM half (the Visibility-tab tables) and calls
in here for the math and the per-session stores.

**One store shape, three settings.** All three are per-camera and live on the
SESSION as a plain `{ cameraName: value }` map (`Session.videoBrightness` /
`videoContrast` / `videoRotation`), because `state.views` is rebuilt from
scratch on every session switch — anything parked on a view silently resets.
Each has a default that is NEVER written to the map or to the `.slp`, so a
project the user never adjusted serializes exactly as before these settings
existed. The four store primitives (`readSetting` / `writeSetting` /
`serializeSetting` / `ingestSetting`) are shared by all three, so a change to
the default-omission rule cannot apply to two of them and silently skip the
third. Brightness and contrast are pure CSS `filter` components; rotation is a
geometric transform the renderer and hit-testing consume via `view.rotation`
(this module owns only its clamp, its store and its serialization).

**The contrast mapping.** CSS `contrast(k)` is linear about mid-grey
(`k = 0` flattens to grey), so the bipolar slider is a straight affine map:
`k = 1 + s / 100` for `s ∈ [-100, 100]` → `k ∈ [0, 2]`, mirroring brightness's
0..200 % → `brightness(0..2)`.

**Key exports.**
- `CONTRAST_MIN` / `CONTRAST_MAX` / `CONTRAST_DEFAULT` (−100 / 100 / 0),
  `BRIGHTNESS_MIN` / `BRIGHTNESS_MAX` / `BRIGHTNESS_DEFAULT` (0 / 200 / 100),
  `ROTATION_MIN` / `ROTATION_MAX` / `ROTATION_DEFAULT` (−179 / 180 / 0).
- `clampContrast(v)` / `clampBrightness(v)` — coerce anything (number, slider
  string, `null`, `NaN`, out-of-range) to a valid integer setting; junk falls
  back to the default rather than producing `NaN`.
- `clampRotation(deg)` — wrap degrees into (−180, 180]. `ui/sessions-panes.js`
  re-exports it. Deliberately does NOT round: the hold-to-rotate loop
  in `ui/ui-wiring.js` advances by a fractional `60 * dt` per frame and needs the
  sub-degree precision to look smooth.
- `clampRotationSetting(v)` — what the store and the `.slp` hold: an INTEGER
  degree in [−179, 180]. Rounds **before** wrapping, because `clampRotation`
  maps into (−180, 180] — an open lower bound — so an input just under −179
  comes back as ~180.9999, and rounding that afterwards would yield 181, one
  past the max. Round-then-wrap is closed under the integer range.
- `contrastFactor(v)` / `brightnessFactor(v)` — slider value → CSS amount.
- `buildVideoFilter(brightness, contrast)` → the COMBINED filter string
  (`''` / `'brightness(1.15)'` / `'brightness(1.15) contrast(0.6)'`). Both
  settings share `canvas.style.filter`, so they must be emitted together —
  writing them separately makes the second assignment erase the first. Identity
  components are omitted, and an all-identity pair yields `''`.
- `getSession{Contrast,Brightness,Rotation}(session, camName)` /
  `setSession{Contrast,Brightness,Rotation}(session, camName, value)` — the
  per-session stores (the SESSION is the source of truth — see above). `set`
  returns the clamped value actually stored and **deletes** default entries.
- `serializeVideo{Contrast,Brightness,Rotation}(session)` → the matching
  `metadata.lucid` payload or **`null`** when nothing is worth writing (writers
  must omit the key on `null`, which is what keeps untouched projects
  byte-identical — `tests/e2e/save-golden-digest.mjs`).
- `ingestVideo{Contrast,Brightness,Rotation}(session, raw)` — merge a saved
  payload in, clamping and dropping anything unusable; tolerates a
  missing/garbage payload (older `.slp` files have no such key). Returns the
  count applied.

Callers normally reach the serialize/ingest half through
`import-export/visibility-metadata.js` rather than one setting at a time.

**Imports from project modules.** None, by design.

**Imported by.** `ui/sessions-panes.js` (the three tables + `applyVideoFilters`
+ `restoreViewRotation`, and the `clampRotation` re-export), `ui/ui-wiring.js`
(`setSessionRotation`, to commit the hold-to-rotate gesture),
`import-export/visibility-metadata.js` (the `metadata.lucid` mapping every
reader and writer goes through), `ui/view-align-modal.js` (`getSessionRotation`
/ `setSessionRotation` / `clampRotationSetting`, writing the aligned angles),
`ui/overlay-export-modal.js` (`buildVideoFilter`,
`getSessionBrightness`, `getSessionContrast`, `getSessionRotation`, so the
export matches the live canvases). Bridged into `tests/test-runner.html` and
covered by `tests/test-video-contrast.js` (contrast) and
`tests/test-visibility-metadata.js` (brightness, rotation); the real-app halves
are `tests/e2e/contrast-slider-roundtrip.mjs` and
`tests/e2e/visibility-settings-roundtrip.mjs`.

---

### ui/ui-wiring.js

**Purpose.** Top-level UI wiring. Builds the menu bar, transport controls,
keyboard handlers, visibility tab, view-mode (grid/single) switching,
playback rate, and re-exports popular helpers like `unlinkGroup`,
`showGroupContextMenu`, `seekToLabeledFrame`, `fitTimelineToData`. Transport
buttons and the Arrow/Home/End keyboard handlers route through
`navigateToFrame` (from `initialization.js`) so frame stepping works in a
video-less skeleton + imported-3D-points project as well as with video. When
there is no `videoController`, play/pause (the `btnPlay` button and the spacebar)
drive a private timer-based stepper (`startNoVideoPlayback` /
`stopNoVideoPlayback` / `toggleNoVideoPlayback`) that advances frames at
`state.fps` over `[0, totalFrames-1]`, rendering each via `navigateToFrame` and
stopping at the last frame; the step transport buttons/keys stop it first.

**Key exports.**
- Menu / setup: `setupMenus`, `setupUI`. The menu bar ends with a **Help**
  dropdown (Docs, Settings) after Hot Keys; Docs / Settings are ALSO direct
  buttons at its right end (`menuBarDocs` / `menuBarSettings`, sharing
  `openDocs` / `openSettings`). `updateTriangulateButtonLabels` names the
  Settings default method in the Triangulate button's tooltip and checks the
  menu's Method radio (the label stays a bare "Triangulate"), refreshed via
  `onDefaultTriangulationMethodChange`. The Speed popover (0.25x–3x) is
  right-aligned to its button. Tracks menu: `Propagate Tracks → IDs`
  (`menuPropagateTracksToIds` — an identity per track, assigned to every group;
  sets `session.trustTracks`) and `Propagate IDs → Tracks`
  (`menuPropagateIdsToTracks` → `Session.propagateIdentitiesToTracks`).
  Tracks ▸ **Check ID Switches (Body Size)…** (`menuCheckSizeSwitches`) calls
  `runIdSwitchChecks({size: true, navigateToFrame})`, and Tracks ▸ **Check ID
  Switches (Images)…** (`menuCheckImageSwitches`) `runIdSwitchChecks({image: true,
  navigateToFrame})`, and Tracks ▸ **Check ID Switches (Coat Brightness)…**
  (`menuCheckBrightnessSwitches`) `runIdSwitchChecks({brightness: true,
  navigateToFrame})`, from `ui/id-switch-modal.js`; `setIdSwitchNavigator` and
  `setIdSwitchRefresher` (the repaint after the tab fixes a switch) are called
  once at setup, as is `setTrackSummaryNavigator` (`ui/track-summary-modal.js`:
  the Track All summary's Identities samples and maps go to a frame).
- Color-by toggle: the Tracks / Identity control is in the top toolbar
  (`colorByTracks` / `colorById`; group `aria-label="Color by"`).
  `updateColorByToggle()` reflects `state.colorByIdentity`. Every change goes
  through `setColorByIdentity` (`ui/color-by.js`) into the one handler
  registered here via `onColorByChange`: active class, `drawAllOverlays`,
  `update3DViewport`, and — since a track's color changes palette with the mode
  — `timeline.recolorTracks()` and the Visibility tab's track swatches
  (`populateTimelineVisibility`). Callers: the buttons, the tracker after Track
  All, and Tracks ▸ Propagate Tracks → IDs.
- Node Trails: two pickers for `state.trailSeconds` — the Tracks ▸ Node Trails
  submenu (`#menuTrailsSubmenu`, `menuTrailsOff` / `menuTrailsQuarter` /
  `menuTrailsHalf` / `menuTrailsSecond` / `menuTrailsTwoSeconds`) and the
  toolbar's **Trails** button (`#tbTrails`). Both are built from
  `TRAIL_PRESETS` (`data-trail-sec`) plus **Custom…** (`data-trail-custom`,
  `menuTrailsCustom`), and both go through `setTrailSeconds`. Custom… opens
  `showCustomTrailModal` (`#trailCustomModal`): **Seconds** and **Frames**
  fields (`#trailCustomSeconds` / `#trailCustomFrames`) that rewrite each
  other; an invalid entry blanks the other and disables Apply (`.modal-error`).
  The field typed LAST is what Apply sets, always stored in seconds (frames as
  `frames / fps` exactly). It opens on the current length kept exact (not
  rounded to its display). A note names the rate and the 500-frame cap when
  exceeded. Enter applies, Esc / Cancel change nothing. It is a dialog because
  both menus open on hover and would close under a drifting pointer. A
  non-preset length checks Custom, whose label names it in both menus.
  `updateTrailChecks` re-reads `state.fps` whenever either menu is entered
  (`mouseenter`/`focusin` on `#trailsDropdown` and `#menuTrailsParent`), since
  the rate changes in many places none of which tell trails; it labels each
  item with its frame count, moves the checkmark in BOTH menus, rewrites the
  button's tooltip, and gives the button `.active` while any trail is on. The
  FPS pill's commit redraws when trails are on. The label stays a bare
  "Trails ▾" to save toolbar width. The button is a `.tri-dropdown` (CSS hover
  menu; clicking the button does nothing). Display state, never saved; not in
  the Defining Plane Mode toolbar lock. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/node-trails-toolbar.mjs` and `tests/test-trail-presets.mjs`.
  **Trail shade.** Both menus hold two radio groups (`.trail-menu-heading`,
  `role="group"`): **Length** and **Shade** — one item per `TRAIL_SHADES` entry
  (`data-trail-shade`, ids `menuTrailsShade<Key>`) with a `.trail-shade-swatch`.
  A pick goes through `setTrailShade` (`state.trailShade`, checkmarks in both
  menus, redraw, status line). Default Dark. Unlike the length, the shade is
  **remembered** in `localStorage` (`nodeTrailShade`, best-effort, read once at
  setup; unknown values keep the default) — display taste; never in the `.slp`.
  Covered by `tests/e2e/node-trail-shade.mjs`.
- Node Style: the four per-section Node Style button groups
  (`visUserNodeStyle` / `visPredNodeStyle` / `visReprojNodeStyle` /
  `vis3dNodeStyle`) reuse the `.line-style-btn` click handler (active toggle +
  `data-value` + `drawAllOverlays` + `saveVisSettings`); they are added to
  `visStyleIds` for persistence/restore. The handler additionally rebuilds the
  3D skeleton for `vis3dNodeStyle` (`viewport3d.skeletonNodeShape = …; setFrame`).
- Visibility defaults: reprojection Brightness (`visReprojBrightness`) **50%**,
  reprojection Node Size **4**, reprojection Edge Style **solid**, predicted
  Node Size **6**, and 3D Viewer Brightness (`vis3dBrightness`) **50%**. The
  same values are mirrored in `overlay-export-layout.js` (`reproj.brightness`,
  `reproj.lineStyle`, `pred.nodeSize`), the `rendering.js`
  `getVisibilitySettings` fallbacks, and `overlays.js` `drawFrameOverlays`. The
  reprojection Brightness slider is **always enabled** (it tints edges and
  labels whatever the Node Color). `vis3dBrightness` is entered as a percentage and wired via
  `skelSizeIds` with `scale: 0.01` into `viewport3d.skeletonBrightness`.
- The visibility `localStorage` blob carries a `_v` version
  (`VIS_CACHE_VERSION`, now 3). Every control is saved on any panel edit, so a
  stored value equal to an OLD default is not a choice: on restore, for each
  `VIS_CACHE_OLD_DEFAULTS` entry newer than the blob, keys still holding that
  entry's old default are dropped so the new default applies (v2:
  `visReprojBrightness` `'100'`; v3: `visReprojNodeSize` `'16'`,
  `visPredNodeSize` `'20'`, `visReprojLineStyle` `'dashed'`). **Changing a
  Visibility default means adding an entry there.**
- File ▸ "Export Video Overlays" (`menuExportOverlayVideo`) is wired to
  `showOverlayExportModal()` (overlay-export-modal.js); it sits directly above
  File ▸ "Export 3D Video" (`menuExportVideo3d`), which is wired to
  `showExport3DVideoModal()` (export-modals.js).
- Session strip: the **"+"** button (`btnAddSession`) calls
  `handleEmptySession()` to create a fresh empty session directly (inheriting the
  shared project skeleton — the user then adds video via File ▸ Load Videos);
  the **"−"** button (`btnRemoveSession`) calls `removeSession`. Folder-based
  session loading stays on the File menu (`menuLoadSessionFolder` →
  `loadSingleSessionFromCache`, `menuLoadMultiSessionFolder`).
- Group ops: `unlinkGroup`, `performGroupButtonAction` (shared by the toolbar
  Group button and the `Shift+G` shortcut — context-sensitive group/ungroup),
  `showGroupContextMenu`, `hideGroupContextMenu`. The context menu's **Delete
  group** (`#ctxDeleteGroup`) removes the members' store rows first —
  `deleteTargetsFromStore(session, groupMemberTargets(frame, group))` from
  `ui/custom-delete-ops.js`, exactly as the Delete key does — then
  `removeInstanceGroup`; memory-only removal is undone on a lazy project by the
  next re-hydration or save. Covered by `tests/e2e/sequence-lazy-workflow.mjs`
  cycle 5c (`D_CTX`).
- Instance copy/paste (`copySelectedInstance` / `pasteInstance`; catalog ids
  `copyInstance` (Mod+C) / `pasteInstance` (Mod+V)). Copy snapshots the
  selected UserInstance in the focused view as a node-name→point map plus the
  skeleton's `compatibilityKey()`. Paste requires the same key, remaps points
  **by node name**, and calls `interactionManager._addNewInstance(points)` at
  the **exact copied coordinates** (may land out of bounds). Occlusion flags
  are not carried (coordinates + per-node visibility are).
- Seekbar: `updateSeekbar`, `updateSeekbarVisual`, `onPlaybackStateChange`. On
  a lazy project, playback runs `lazyPlaybackLoader`: it keeps
  `LAZY_PLAYBACK_AHEAD` frames hydrated ahead and calls `evictLazyFrames(cur)`
  after each top-up (`pose/lazy-residency.js`); Play, Space and the seekbar's
  resume preload the same window. The lookahead and the eviction's protected
  ahead-window are one constant, so they cannot drift apart.
  **A click or drag on the seekbar keeps the video playing or paused, as it
  was.** `scrubToFrame` always stops playback; a press that began while playing
  (or while `videoController.resumePending`) calls
  `videoController.resumeAfterScrub` on release. A video-less project does the
  same with its timer. Covered by `tests/e2e/seekbar-click-keeps-playback.mjs`.
- Toggles: `toggleInfoPanel`, `refreshInfoPanelAfterShow`,
  `updateInfoPanelToggleBtn`, `toggle3DViewport`,
  `update3DViewportToggleBtn`, `toggleTimeline`,
  `syncTimelineToggleButton`, `fitTimelineToData`.
- View modes: `enterSingleViewMode`, `cycleSingleView`, `setSoloView`,
  `setGridMode`, `updateVideoGridDisplay`, `showViewIndicator`. See
  **Single-view ("solo") mode** below.
- Playback: `applyPlaybackRate`, `seekToLabeledFrame`.
- View ▸ **Native Resolution (1:1)** (`menuNativeResolution`) and **`Shift+0`**
  (matched by `e.code === 'Digit0'` in the transport keydown handler, beside
  `+` / `-` / `0`, so only while real video is loaded) both call
  `videoController.toggleNativeResolution()` (`loading/video.js`). The menu
  tick (`.trail-check`) is refreshed when the View menu opens, from
  `nativeResolutionActive()` (derived from the views, so it cannot go stale).
  `updateVideoGridDisplay` and the `setGridMode` layout restore carry each
  view's `zoom.native` across the pane rebuild (a restored 1:1 view gets
  `reapplyZoom`, not the old box's scale).
- View ▸ **Define Planes** (`menuDefinePlanes`) and **`Mod+Shift+P`**
  (`definePlanes`) both call the SAME `togglePlaneMode()`
  (`ui/plane-definition.js`) — exiting has unwinding to do, so a second entry
  point would be a second place to forget it. A mode, not a modal: no Esc
  binding. `p` alone is Toggle Predictions, kept apart only by `matchChord`'s
  bare-letter-needs-shift-UP rule. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/define-planes-shortcut.mjs`.
- Help ▸ **Hot Keys** (`menuHotkeys`) and **`?`** (`showHotkeys`) →
  `showHotkeysHelp()`, rendered from `getActions()`. It must stay at **module
  scope**: its two callers are in different closures (`setupMenus`,
  `setupUI`'s `setHandler`), and declaring it inside `setupMenus` makes `?`
  throw a `ReferenceError`. It returns early if `#hotkeysClose` exists
  (`dispatchEvent` does not consume the keydown, so a second `?` would stack an
  overlay). Deliberately **no `case '?'` in the plain-key switch** — it would
  open a duplicate. Covered by `tests/e2e/define-planes-shortcut.mjs` §5.

**Panel toggles: independent sizing, and hidden means idle.** `toggleInfoPanel`
(`I`) and `toggle3DViewport` (`\`) each only flip their own panel's `collapsed`
class. **Neither resizes the other** — `.video-grid-section` is the only
`flex: 1` child of `.main-content`, so it absorbs and releases the space. An
inline width **outranks** `.collapsed { width: 0 }`, so both toggles *park* any
inline width before adding `collapsed` and restore it after removing it
(`_savedWidth` on `#viewport3dContainer`, `_savedWidth`/`_savedMinWidth` on
`#infoPanel` — the split handles write inline widths there).

Each toggle also drives the work — see `ui/panel-visibility.js`. Hiding the 3D
viewport goes through `collapseViewport3D` (shared with Track All):
`Viewport3D.setVisible(false)` stops the render loop and defers rebuilds;
showing calls `setVisible(true)` then `update3DViewport(state.currentFrame)`,
which also auto-inits a viewport released while collapsed. Showing the info
panel calls `refreshInfoPanelAfterShow`, which rebuilds **only if** a refresh
was skipped (`consumeInfoPanelStale`). Covered by
`tests/e2e/panel-toggle-independence.mjs`.

**Toolbar toggle buttons.** `#viewport3dToggleBtn` ("3D") and
`#infoPanelToggleBtn` ("Panel") sit at the toolbar's far right in
`.toolbar-group.panel-toggles` (`.panel-toggle-btn`, outlined).
`update3DViewportToggleBtn` / `updateInfoPanelToggleBtn` derive state from the
container's `collapsed` class, not from whoever toggled, so every entry point
stays in sync; they are called from the toggle **and** from the
`MutationObserver` in `ui/layout-controls.js` (which also sets the initial
state). Labels are short and **fixed** (a changing label would shove its
neighbour); `.active` / `aria-pressed` while shown, tooltip says what a click
will do (private `syncPanelToggleBtn`). The toolbar must fit a 1440 px window
(~1,380 px used) — `tests/e2e/toolbar-3d-toggle-button.mjs` asserts it.

**Single-view ("solo") mode.** `v` (`singleViewMode`) → `enterSingleViewMode`:
caches the dockview layout (`savedGridLayout`), sets `state.viewMode =
'single'`, shows only `interactionManager.lastInteractedView`'s pane. `v` again
is a deliberate **no-op**. Cycling walks `state.views` (the view strip's
order):

- **`↑` / `↓`** → `cycleSingleView(-1 | +1)`, wrapping. A **dedicated** keydown
  listener, not catalog-dispatched: the dispatcher `preventDefault()`s every
  binding it matches, which would swallow the arrows app-wide. The catalog
  carries a reference-only `soloCycleView` entry. Each step calls
  `scrollViewStripTo`.
- **A single click in the view strip** → `setSoloView(name)`
  (`ui/sessions-panes.js`), which returns false outside solo mode so the strip
  falls back to focusing the pane.

`g` (`setGridMode`) is a **no-op when already in grid mode** — the restore is a
full `clearAll()` + `fromJSON()`, and a repeated `g` would hand the selection
to whichever panel dockview activated. Out of solo it restores the cached
layout, then `activatePanelForView` on the solo'd view, so the strip
highlight, `lastInteractedView` and the 3D camera highlight all follow from
that one `setActive()`. `api.fromJSON()` builds panels behind `addVideoPanel`'s
back, so `paneManager.syncDockedViews()` runs first.

**The cached layout is VALIDATED at the point of use, not invalidated at each
mutation.** `state.views` can change while solo (remove/load a video, switch
sessions); restoring a stale snapshot would rebuild an empty pane for a removed
camera, or omit an added one. `savedGridLayoutMatchesViews()` compares the
snapshot's panel set with the live views as a SET (both directions), and
`setGridMode` falls back to `addAllViewsAsGrid()` on a mismatch. One reader,
so validating there beats a list of invalidation sites something can be left
off. It reads `params.viewName` from dockview's documented `toJSON` shape, not
private internals.

**A view's zoom and pan survive every one of these rebuilds** — the zoom lives
on the view, and a rebuild is treated like a resize: same magnification, same
region centred, pan rescaled to the new cell (`refreshPaneInteractions` →
`reapplyZoom`). Exception: while Native Resolution is on, a camera coming on
screen joins at 1:1. There is deliberately **no save/restore of the zoom
around the rebuild** in `setGridMode` / `updateVideoGridDisplay`: writing the
OLD cell's pan back over the rescaled one shifts the view. Covered by
`tests/e2e/zoom-survives-pane-rebuild.mjs` (§7) and
`tests/e2e/native-resolution-zoom.mjs` §12.

Solo mode is covered by `tests/e2e/solo-view-navigation.mjs` and
`tests/e2e/videos-panel-buttons.mjs` §7; `tests/test-view-mode.js` only
simulates the index arithmetic.

**Visibility panel — the global/session split.** `saveVisSettings` /
`restoreVisSettings` cache the **global appearance preferences**
(`visSliderIds` / `visCheckIds` / `visStyleIds`) in
`localStorage.visibilitySettings` — browser-local display taste, deliberately
**not** in the `.slp` (a colleague's project must not reassign your node
sizes). The *session-scoped* settings (per-camera brightness / contrast /
rotation, timeline hidden sets) persist per session via
`import-export/visibility-metadata.js` and never touch localStorage.

The **`Planes` section**'s four toggles (`PLANE_VIS_IDS`, imported from
`ui/plane-visibility.js` so the two cannot drift) are wired apart from the
other checkboxes: they need BOTH `drawAllOverlays` and `syncPlanes3D`, and the
pose deselect sweep has nothing to do for them.

**Hold-to-rotate (`Shift+R` + `←`/`→`).** `rotationLoop` advances
`view.rotation` by a fractional `60 * dt` per frame, outside the session store.
The gesture is committed **once, on keyup**: `setSessionRotation` stores the
rounded degree, `view.rotation` snaps to exactly that (what renders is what a
reopen renders), and `markDirty()` fires.

`redrawForRotationDegree(view)` repaints overlays only when
`Math.round(view.rotation)` changes (`_rotState.drawnDeg`; null forces the
first paint, keyup clears it so the snapped angle repaints). Labels cancel the
view's rotation, so without it labels would spin until keyup; whole degrees is
the granularity labels are drawn at (`ui/rendering.js`).

The chord's "don't also step frames" guard sits **before** the `hasRealVideo()`
branch in the arrow-key handler, so it holds on a project with no decoder.

`showViewIndicator` also toggles **`has-view-indicator`** on `#videoDock`, so
the sole pane's Display Legend steps down a slot instead of hiding under the
chip (styles.css). Removed on every path that hides the chip, or the legend
would sit low in grid mode.

**Imports from project modules.** Nearly every other module — see file
header for the full list. Notable ones: `app-state.js`,
`timeline-controller.js`, `pose-data.js`, `triangulation.js`
(incl. `evictLazyFrames`), `lazy-residency.js` (`LAZY_PLAYBACK_AHEAD`),
`rendering.js`, `info-panel.js`, `save-load.js`, `slp-import.js`,
`file-io.js`, `session-loader.js`, `video.js`, `tracker.js`,
`initialization.js`, `identity-assignment.js` (the track/identity helpers,
Edit Group, `startManualAssignment`, `purgeTriangulationDataForGroup`,
`swapTracks`), `export-modals.js`,
`sessions-panes.js`, `settings.js`, `settings-modal.js`,
`track-range-modal.js` (`showTrackRangeModal`, the `#tbTrackFrameRange`
dropdown item),
`animal-count-field.js` (`wireAnimalCountField`, the Track group's Animals
field),
`view-align-modal.js` (`showAlignViewsModal`, View ▸ "Align Views to
References…"),
`seekbar-tooltip.js` (`installSeekbarTooltip`),
`seekbar-preview.js` (`installSeekbarPreview` — the selected view's thumbnail
above the tooltip, handed to it as `preview`),
`frame-readout.js` (`showReadoutFrame`, `refreshReadoutTotals` — the
time / frame readout, written by `updateSeekbarVisual`, the inline frame editor
and the FPS pill),
`seekbar-markers.js` (`installSeekbarMarkers`, `seekbarMarkerAt`,
`describeSwitchMarker`, `setSeekbarMarkerFrames` — the possible-ID-switch ticks:
the scrub handlers and the tooltip snap to a tick within 5 px),
`seekbar-ticks.js` (`installSeekbarTicks`, `refreshSeekbarTicks` — the time
ruler under the seekbar; `updateSeekbarVisual` calls the refresh, a no-op
unless the frame count or FPS changed),
`color-by.js` (`onColorByChange`, `setColorByIdentity` — the Tracks /
Identity toggle),
`trail-presets.js` (`TRAIL_PRESETS`, `MAX_TRAIL_FRAMES`, `trailPresetFor`,
`trailLabel`, `trailFrames`, `trailRate`, `formatTrailSeconds`,
`trailSecondsForFrames`, `parseTrailSeconds`, `parseTrailFrames`,
`setTrailSeconds`, `onTrailSecondsChange` — the Node Trails menus and their
Custom… dialog; the change handler is registered here, so a length set by Track
All syncs the menus too),
`overlays.js` (`TRAIL_SHADES`, `TRACK_COLORS`, `trailShadeFor`, `trailColor` —
the menus' Shade group and its swatches),
`video-filters.js` (`setSessionRotation`; `clampRotation` still comes in via
`sessions-panes.js`, which re-exports it), `plane-definition.js`
(`togglePlaneMode`).

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`,
`ui/layout-controls.js`, `ui/sessions-panes.js` (`setSoloView`),
`loading/session-loader.js`, `import-export/slp-import.js`.

**User-facing features.** Menu bar (File / Edit / Tracks / View / Hot Keys,
plus a right-aligned Help menu), transport controls (play/pause/seek/speed), keyboard shortcuts (Space,
arrows, T, A, etc.), grid/single view toggle, info-panel/3D/timeline
visibility toggles, "seek to next labeled frame".

**Help menu + Settings.** The right-aligned **Help** menu: `menuDocumentation`
opens the docs site (`https://talmolab.github.io/luc3d-docs/`) in a new tab;
`menuSettings` → `showSettingsModal()`. Tracks ▸ `menuTrackingWizard` and
**`Mod+Shift+I`** (`openTrackingWizard`) → `showSettingsModal('wizard')`. The
**Hot Keys** modal is generated from `getActions()`, so it follows the catalog
and user rebindings.

**Toolbar layout.** After Sessions come two groups, one per kind of work:
manual labelling (`#tbLabellingGroup`: Instance ▾, Group ▾), then
proofreading in the order it runs (`#tbProofreadingGroup`: Track ▾,
Triangulate ▾, Smooth ▾, then the triangulation badge). Labels are short to
save width ("Smooth" is Temporal Smoothing; the tooltip says so). Covered by
`tests/e2e/toolbar-hover-menus.mjs` §4.

**Track, Triangulate and Smooth share one menu shape**
(`wireActionMenu(dropdownId, handlers)`). Each is ONE button whose click runs
**all frames**; its `:hover` menu (`.tri-dropdown.action-menu`) has **This
frame**, **All frames** (Track also **Frame range…**), the action's quick
settings, and **Settings…** for its Settings panel. The All frames item
forwards to the button (`button.click()`), so the two cannot differ and a
disabled button disables both; other items call `handlers[data-scope]`. Each
button keeps the id of the old all-frames button (`#tbTriangulateAll`,
`#tbTrackAll`), so a click on it does what it always did; This frame is
`#tbTriangulate` / `#tbSmoothFrame` / `#tbTrackFrame`. All items are
`<button>`s so Defining Plane Mode can disable the runs one by one while the
settings in the same menu stay live. `:focus-within` keeps a menu open, so
typing in Animals does not lose it and Tab walks into the items.
- **Triangulate ▾** (`#triangulateDropdown`): a bare "Triangulate"; the
  tooltip names the method a click runs (#138); the Method radios (`input[name="tbTriMethod"]`) ARE Settings ▸ Default
  Triangulation (`setDefaultTriangulationMethod`; `updateTriangulateButtonLabels`
  re-checks them on `onDefaultTriangulationMethodChange`). This frame runs
  `triangulateCurrentFrame(default)`; Settings… → `'triangulation'`. Implicit
  triangulation (`t`, Edit ▸ Triangulate) and the environment-skeleton solve
  (Load Environment, via `resolveTriangulationMethod(group)` on a new group)
  use the default too.
- **Smooth ▾** (`#smoothingDropdown`, `#tbSmooth`; Temporal Smoothing): also
  has the **Remove smoothing / Restore smoothing** toggle (`#tbSmoothToggle`,
  `data-scope="toggle"`) after All frames; see below.
  Settings… → `'smoothing'`.
- **Track ▾** (`#trackDropdown`): the button (Track All) and This frame keep
  their own handlers in `pose/tracker.js`'s IIFE; `ui-wiring.js` wires Frame
  range… to `showTrackRangeModal()`, passing `navigateToFrame` as `onTracked`
  (injected to avoid the `initialization.js` → `ui-wiring.js` → modal cycle),
  and Settings… → `'wizard'`. The Animals field sits in this menu.
The menus are pure `:hover`: in a test, a modal opening over the toolbar drops
hover, so a second click must re-hover the button first
(`tests/e2e/track-frame-range.mjs`). Shape covered by
`tests/e2e/toolbar-hover-menus.mjs` §5.

**Instance and Group are hover menus.** To save toolbar width, `+ Instance` /
`- Instance` sit under an **Instance ▾** button (`#tbInstanceMenu`,
`#instanceDropdown`) and `Group` / `Edit Group` under **Group ▾**
(`#tbGroupMenu`, `#groupDropdown`), opening on `:hover`. The four items are
still the original `<button>`s with their ids and handlers
(`button.tri-dropdown-item` restyles them as rows), so `ui/rendering.js` keeps
rewriting Group's label and both items' `disabled` / `.active`; it also turns
`#tbGroupMenu` blue whenever either item is. Tab skips the items while the menu
is closed; their shortcuts (`Mod+I`, `Delete`, `Shift+G`) still work.
Covered by `tests/e2e/toolbar-hover-menus.mjs`.

**Temporal Smoothing** (luc3d #134) runs the private async
`runTemporalSmoothing(currentFrameOnly, toggle)` (a re-entry guard drops a
second click while one runs). The menu's toggle (`#tbSmoothToggle`,
`toggle`), for comparing with and without: while anything is smoothed,
**Remove smoothing** restores the pre-smoothing 3D on every frame and keeps
what it took off (`removeSmoothing`, with the settings as they are then);
otherwise **Restore smoothing** (`restoreSmoothing`) copies it back with no
recompute and no card, or, when the settings or any 3D changed since,
smooths again with the current settings in the removed run's scope (all
frames, or `smoothFrame` on each removed this-frame run's frames), its status
ending "— recomputed…". "Nothing is smoothed" if neither applies. Otherwise
it reads `getSmoothingParams()`; when
both kernels are off it restores the pre-smoothing 3D (`unsmoothSession`), or, with nothing
smoothed, says so and opens Settings ▸ Temporal Smoothing rather than silently
doing nothing; otherwise All frames runs `smoothSessionAsync`
(`pose/temporal-smoothing.js`; every run filters the pre-smoothing 3D, so it
replaces the last) behind the shared progress card, counted in POSES (one per
animal per frame), shown at once and painted (`yieldToPaint`) before the pass.
This frame runs `smoothFrame(session, params, state.currentFrame)`
synchronously with no card: it reads only the frames within the filter windows
of the current one (~1–4 ms on 200,000 frames, vs ~1.3 s when it filtered whole
trajectories) and writes the values an All frames run gives that frame. Then it
records the scope (`noteSmoothingScope`), drops the `state.triangulationResults`
entries of every group whose reprojections the pass cleared (the fill appends a
fresh one, and the panel would average both; This frame checks only its own
frame), and runs `markDirty` + `drawAllOverlays` + `update3DViewport(state.currentFrame)`
(the frame matters: without it the 3D view got no groups and went blank) +
`updateInfoPanel`, setting the summary status last. Both-windows-0 restores
pass `writeFrames: new Set([state.currentFrame])` for This frame. It touches only `points3d` on the
already-resident `session.instanceGroups`, hydrating no 2D. The button is blue
while smoothing is applied, and the menu lights only the last run's scope
(`ui/smoothing-button.js`, synced on every overlay draw).

**The Animals field (`#tbNumAnimals`)** is wired by `wireAnimalCountField()`
from `ui/animal-count-field.js`, which owns it (see that entry).

**Track / Identity menu modals.** Tracks ▸ New / Rename / Delete for tracks
and identities use private helpers taking `kind = 'track' | 'identity'`, sharing
`.rename-list` styling (`.rename-list-item.selected`) and the
`.multi-frame-modal` shell; all close on Esc:
- `showCreateModal(kind)` — read-only reference list + "New name" entry;
  Enter creates; validates non-empty + duplicate.
- `showRenameModal(kind)` — single-select list + new-name entry; renames
