  `session.tracks` / `session.identities[].name` and migrates hidden-set
  membership; Enter applies.
- `showCustomDeleteModal()` — **Edit ▸ "Custom Instance Delete…"**
  (`#menuCustomDeleteInstance`), SLEAP's Labels ▸ Custom Instance Delete…
  Selects: `Delete` (predicted (default) / user / all), `Grouping` (any /
  grouped / ungrouped — ORTHOGONAL to type), `in` (current frame / current
  session — LUCID's analogue of "current video"), `in view` (all / one camera),
  plus `with track` / `with identity` when the session has any. A live count, a
  per-camera table with Total, and a **cascade line** ("N group(s) removed · N
  ungrouped · N lose their 3D · N predicted instance(s) promoted to User"),
  because the ≥2-member invariant makes those consequences surprising and
  irreversible. Delete is an explicit click (never Enter). On apply it
  re-collects (the model can move under an open dialog), clears the selection
  FIRST (a stale `selectedInstanceGroup` would point at a deleted object, and
  `viewport3d.selectedInstanceIdx` is positional), runs `executeDeletion`, then
  `purgeTriangulationDataForGroup` over `purgedGroups` (the ops module is
  import-free), and reports the **durable** store-row count, surfacing
  `errorRows` as a warning. All logic is in `ui/custom-delete-ops.js`. No
  shortcut (as in SLEAP), so no catalog entry. Deliberately does NOT copy
  SLEAP's `labels.clean()` cascade (pruning unused tracks/skeletons) — data
  loss by surprise given LUCID's explicit `Delete Track…` and one skeleton per
  project. Covered by `tests/e2e/custom-delete-modal.mjs`.
- `showDeleteModal(kind)` — single-select list, a red `.delete-warning`, and a
  per-camera table of instances to be nulled with a `.delete-total-row`.
  Delete (`.danger`) is an explicit click, NOT bound to Enter.
Count + delete logic is in `ui/track-identity-ops.js` (see that entry). All
apply paths refresh overlays / info panel / timeline (`keepSize`) / visibility.

**Catalog-driven keyboard shortcuts.** Every **standard single-action**
shortcut is dispatched (`setHandler(id, fn)` + one `keydown` listener calling
`dispatchEvent(e)`), so it is **rebindable** from Settings: the plain-key
toggles (`u`/`p`/`r`/`e`, `v`, `g`, `t`, `n`, `i`, `\`, `?`, `Shift+G`,
`Shift+U`, `f`), `Shift+T`, `Mod+Shift+T`, `Mod+Shift+I`, `Mod+I`, `Mod+,`,
`Mod+O`. `Shift+G` calls the **same** `performGroupButtonAction()` as the
toolbar Group button. The rest keep dedicated handlers and are **fixed**
reference entries: `Mod+S` (works while typing), transport (`←/→`, `Space`,
`Home`/`End`, `Opt+←/→`), `1–9` / `Shift+1–9`, zoom (`+`/`-`/`0`, `Shift+0`),
`Shift+R`+rotate, `Delete` and the legacy `c` (`groupConfirmLegacy`, in
`interaction.js`), and `Mod+J`/`Mod+Shift+J` (timeline-controller).
`Enter`/`Escape` remain hard-coded modal-button cases.

**One focus guard, not ten copies.** The shortcut listeners here (and in
`ui/settings.js`, `ui/interaction.js`, `loading/video.js`) ask
`shouldIgnoreShortcut(e)` (`ui/keyboard-target.js`) rather than testing
`tagName === 'INPUT'`, which was also true for a CHECKBOX. `setupUI` first
calls `installFocusRelease(document)`, which hands focus back after a POINTER
activates a checkbox, radio or button; keyboard focus is left alone, so
Tab + `Space` still toggles.

**Visibility wiring + rename migration.** Every track/identity add / rename /
delete handler that calls `timeline.refreshTracks` also calls
`populateTimelineVisibility(state.session)`. The rename handlers call
`renameHiddenTrack` / `renameHiddenIdentity` **before** the rename is applied,
so a hidden entity stays hidden under its new name.


---

### ui/viewport3d.js

**Purpose.** Three.js 3D viewport that renders triangulated skeletons,
camera frustum wireframes, skeleton edges, camera position labels.
Self-contained — caller passes `cameras`, `skeleton`, color callbacks
via the options bag.

**Key exports.**
- `Viewport3D` — class. Selected methods: `setFrame(instanceGroups)`,
  `setSelectedInstance`, `setEnvironment`, `clearEnvironment`,
  `setPlanes`, `clearPlanes`, `setMeshMembership`, `clearMeshMembership`,
  `setSelectedPlaneNode`, `clearSelectedPlaneNode`,
  `setAnglePreview`, `clearAnglePreview`, `setPlaneRoles`, `clearPlaneRoles`,
  `setOriginPickMode`, `setOriginCandidates`,
  `clearOriginCandidates`, `setOriginFrame`, `clearOriginFrame`,
  `addCameraPyramids`, `selectCamera`, `showSelectedCameraView`,
  `showInitialView`, `setMissingVideoCameras`, `highlightCamera`,
  `resize`, `resetCamera`, `lookAtOrigin`, `fitToScene`, `originPivot`,
  `originUp`, `setVisible(visible)` / `isVisible()`, `dispose`.
- **`skeleton` may be null** (`newProject`, removing the last session) and the
  loaders seek — which draws — before they set the new one; `updateSkeleton`
  and `setEnvironment` draw a null skeleton as one with no edges rather than
  throw (`tests/e2e/new-project-then-load.mjs`).
- **`setVisible(false)` stops ALL processing** (the `\` toggle's other half —
  see `ui/panel-visibility.js`); a collapsed container is still a WebGL render
  target, so an unpaused `_animate()` would render ~60x/second for nothing.
  Hiding cancels `_rafId` (and the camera fly-in's separate rAF loop) and routes
  every scene rebuild — `setFrame`/`setSelectedInstance`, `addCameraPyramids`,
  `setEnvironment`/`clearEnvironment`, `highlightCamera`, `fitToScene` — into
  `_deferred`, at most one thunk per `DEFER_REPLAY_ORDER` key (scrubbing 10,000
  frames while hidden leaves ONE `'frame'` rebuild). Nothing is disposed, so the
  scene, WebGL context and orbit pose survive. `setVisible(true)` replays in
  `DEFER_REPLAY_ORDER` (geometry first: `'highlight'` needs
  `addCameraPyramids`' meshes, `'fit'` needs `setFrame`'s skeleton), then
  resizes (every `resize()` early-returned on the 0x0 container).
  `setEnvironment` is the one deferral that captures `this.skeleton`
  explicitly: callers set an env-specific skeleton, call in, and restore the
  normal one on the next line. `visible` is a per-instance constructor option
  (default `true`), not a DOM read, because the two export-modal instances must
  keep rendering regardless of the main panel.

**Scene groups.** Ten `THREE.Group` siblings under the scene: `_cameraGroup`,
`_skeletonGroup`, `_envGroup`, `_planeGroup`, `_meshMembershipGroup`,
`_nodeSelectGroup` (the selected-node rings), `_angleGroup` (the Set Angle ghost), `_planeRoleGroup` (the Set Angle role
outlines), `_originGroup` (the Set Origin candidate arrows) and `_framePivot`
(the grid floor + axis helper).
**`updateSkeleton` clears ONLY `_skeletonGroup`**, every frame — that is the
entire mechanism by which the environment overlay and annotated planes persist.
Anything frame-independent must be a sibling, never a child of
`_skeletonGroup`. Every data group hangs off `scene`, i.e. is drawn in
**calibration world**; only `_framePivot` is in the user's origin frame.

**Plane corners size independently of pose nodes** — `setPlanes` uses
`this.planeNodeSize` (the panel's "3D Node Size" slider, via `syncPlanes3D`), not
`skeletonNodeSize`.

**`showPlaneSurfaces` / `showPlaneNodes`** (both default `true`) decide whether
`setPlanes` builds each plane's BODY (edges + fill) and its CORNERS — the
Visibility panel's "Planes / Nodes in 3D Views" toggles, pushed by
`syncPlanes3D` and forced on inside Defining Plane Mode. They **never filter
`_planes`**: a live drag, `setSelectedPlaneNode`, `setMeshMembership` and
`setPlaneRoles` all resolve ids against it, so hiding a plane costs meshes and
nothing else. With corners off nothing is pickable, which is correct — the
payload is only `editable` inside the mode, where both flags are on.

**`setPlanes(planes)` / `clearPlanes()`** — user-annotated planes from
`ui/plane-definition.js`'s `syncPlanes3D`. Full rebuild per call. Payload per
plane: `{id, name, color, nodeIds, nodeColors, nodeImmutable, edges,
polygonOrder, filled, editable, planeFit, fitted, points3d}` — `nodeIds` are the
POOL ids behind the corners (parallel to `nodeColors`), `planeFit` is a
**usable** fit (stored or derived; the drag surface) and `fitted` is whether the
user actually clicked Fit (what Set Origin asks). Nodes are spheres in their own
colour; `filled` adds a fan over `polygonOrder` built by `_buildPlaneFillMesh`
(`DoubleSide`, `depthWrite: false` so the fill never hides nodes behind it). A
fan is right for a convex ring; a user-drawn CONCAVE ring can still fan wrong,
the price of honouring their edges. **`points3d` needs no transform** — the
scene is Z-up in the calibration's world frame, like skeleton nodes and camera
centres. `clearPlanes()` also clears the angle ghost, the role outlines and the
selected-node marker, which all resolve through `_planes`.

**`setAnglePreview(spec)` / `clearAnglePreview()`** — a GHOST of where **Set
Angle Between Two Planes** would put the rotated plane, live while its dialog is
open. Its own `_angleGroup`; nothing touches `_planeGroup`. Geometry is the
caller's PLANNED points (written to no node); edges, ring and colour are read
from the last `setPlanes` payload by `spec.planeId`, and the fill reuses
`_buildPlaneFillMesh`, so the ghost is drawn exactly like the real plane.
**`depthTest: false` on purpose** — the useful thing is the DIFFERENCE from the
plane it would replace, which would otherwise hide it. Pure display.

**`setPlaneRoles(roles)` / `clearPlaneRoles()`** — fat, see-through outlines
saying WHICH planes the Set Angle dialog's two dropdowns name and which is held.
`roles` is `[{planeId, color}]`; `null` or `[]` clears. Separate from the ghost
because they clear at different times (a refused plan drops the ghost while the
roles still stand). Geometry is the plane AS IT IS, from the last `setPlanes`
payload; corners are drawn too, so a plane with no edges still reads.
`renderOrder` 8, under the ghost's 9/10. Pure display.

**`setMeshMembership(payload)` / `clearMeshMembership()`** — highlighting for
the SELECTED 3D Mesh Object, pushed by `syncPlanes3D`. Payload `{planeIds}`;
`null` clears. Its own `_meshMembershipGroup`. **It draws the member PLANES, not
a derived body**: each member's FACE is filled and its corners and edges redrawn,
fatter, in `MESH_MEMBER_COLOR` (one fixed yellow).
- **The face is the part that reads.** Built by `_buildPlaneFillGeometry` — the
  plane fill's own ring-walking fan, split out so the highlight can never differ
  in shape — at opacity 0.45 (vs the fill's 0.28), whatever the plane's own
  `filled` flag says. `depthTest` ON for the face (else a cage's back walls
  paint over its front), OFF for corners and outline (an occluded member still
  shows).
- **The colour is fixed, and the payload has NO colour field** — a highlight
  must read against any plane colours, and an unused field would invite wiring
  the object's colour back in. The object's colour lives in the table swatch,
  the editor and the `.glb` `baseColorFactor`.
- **It cannot drift.** Positions come from `this._planes`. The object's DERIVED
  geometry (`pose/mesh-object-geometry.js`) is in the user's **origin frame**
  while this group is in calibration world, so it must never be pushed here.
  Pinned by `tests/e2e/mesh-membership-highlight.mjs`.
- A deleted member plane simply lights nothing (lazy resolution, no throw).
  `renderOrder` 7: above plane fills (0), under the role outlines (8).

**`setSelectedPlaneNode(payload)` / `clearSelectedPlaneNode()`** — the ONE plane
node selected in the Nodes table, pushed by `syncPlanes3D`'s
`syncSelectedNode3D`. Payload `{nodeId}`; `null` clears. Its own
`_nodeSelectGroup`.
- **A pool node ID crosses the boundary, never a position** —
  `_findPlaneNodePoint` resolves it against `this._planes`, for the same
  origin-vs-calibration-frame reason as `setMeshMembership`.
- **One marker, even for a shared corner** (first plane carrying the id wins;
  every plane draws that node at the same position).
- **Three rings in the three coordinate planes, EMPTY middle.** Anything solid
  over a dozen-pixel corner hides the node's own colour; three orthogonal rings
  cannot all be edge-on and need no per-frame re-aiming. `NODE_SELECT_COLOR` is
  fixed white, since a marker in the node's colour is invisible on it.
- **A node in no plane is marked nowhere** — the 3D view draws planes only.
  `depthTest: false`, `renderOrder` 7. Pinned by
  `tests/e2e/plane-node-selection.mjs`.

**Dragging a plane corner in 3D** (`_setupPlaneEditing` and friends;
callbacks `onPlaneNodeDragged(planeId, nodeIdx, [x,y,z])` /
`onPlaneNodeDragEnd(planeId, nodeIdx)`, wired by `syncPlanes3D`). Two rules,
both enforced here rather than by the caller:
1. **Only a plane WITH A FIT is draggable, and never a PINNED corner.**
   `setPlanes` stamps
   `userData.planeEditable = editable && planeFit && !nodeImmutable[k]` on each
   corner mesh and the raycast only collects those. `planeFit` is the
   **usable** fit, so an un-Fit plane with three solved corners is draggable too.
   The `nodeImmutable` term matters because `planeEditable` also gates the
   hover cursor (`_pickPlaneNode`); without it a pinned corner would advertise a
   drag that `onPlaneNodeDragged` refuses.
   **`userData.planeFitted` comes from the payload's separate `fitted` flag**
   and does not follow `planeEditable`: Set Origin asks whether the user has
   DECLARED the plane's frame, so a derived surface must not make an un-Fit
   plane eligible, and a pinned corner is a *preferred* origin anchor and must
   stay pickable. A payload without `fitted` falls back to `!!planeFit`.
2. **A corner can only move WITHIN its fitted plane.** The pointer ray is
   intersected with a `THREE.Plane` from the fit's normal + centroid, so the
   result is on the plane by construction. Near-parallel rays (`|cos| < 1e-3`)
   are refused rather than flung to a huge coordinate.

The fit is held **fixed** for the drag and not re-derived after it: centroid +
normal become the origin's translation + rotation, so a nudge must not move the
frame it defines (and a mid-drag re-fit would chase the dragged corner).

The `pointerdown` listener is **capture-phase on `container`, not on the
canvas** — OrbitControls registered its canvas `pointerdown` first, and at the
target element listeners fire in registration order, so only an ancestor's
capture phase genuinely precedes it and can `stopPropagation()`.
`controls.enabled` is also flipped for the duration. `_suppressCameraClick`
swallows the `click` after a drag released over a camera pyramid (which would
jump into that camera's view). `dispose()` removes the container listener, which
outlives the viewport.

**Set Origin support** (`setOriginPickMode` / `setOriginCandidates` /
`setOriginFrame`, driven by `ui/origin-definition.js`).
- `setOriginPickMode('node'|'axis'|null)` arms picking. While armed the click
  handler **consumes the click even on a miss**, so a stray click cannot select
  a camera and swing the view away. `'node'` only collects meshes flagged
  `userData.planeFitted`.
- `setOriginCandidates({origin, normal, length, chosen})` draws both ±normal
  arrows as real shaft+head **meshes** — not `ArrowHelper`, whose `Line` shaft
  raycasts unreliably. `chosen` dims the loser instead of removing it. `length`
  comes from the caller so it scales to the plane.
- `setOriginFrame(frame)` moves `_framePivot` (grid + axes) onto the user's
  frame via `makeBasis` (basis **columns**). Only the frame and the ORBIT move;
  no data group is touched.

**The orbit follows the applied origin** (`originPivot` / `originUp` /
`_rebaseControls`, `_frameDirection`). Re-basing only the grid would leave drag,
pan and wheel pivoting on an invisible calibration origin. `_rebaseControls`
moves `controls.target` to the frame's origin and `camera.up` to its +Z, and
translates the camera by the same delta so the **view does not jump**.
`setOriginFrame(null)` is symmetric.

**Why the controls get REBUILT, not just re-aimed.** r147's OrbitControls
captures its orbit-axis quaternion from `camera.up` once, at construction, so a
later `camera.up = …` does nothing for the orbit. `_createControls` owns
construction (and the `'change'` → `_checkDeclutter` and `'start'` listeners,
so a rebuild keeps them) and records the axis in force as `_controlsUp`;
`_rebaseControls` rebuilds only when the axis really moved.
`minDistance`/`maxDistance`/`enabled` carry across.

**`resetCamera` / `fitToScene` / `showInitialView` are frame-relative.** Their
canned angles are stated in FRAME coordinates and rotated out through
`_frameDirection`. With a frame applied `fitToScene` fits **around the frame's
origin** rather than the point cloud's centroid — framing one point while
orbiting another makes the first drag swing the scene off-centre.

**Picking calls `scene.updateMatrixWorld()` first.** Raycasting reads
`matrixWorld`, otherwise only refreshed by `render()`, and the origin arrows are
built in response to the PREVIOUS click — so a click before the next frame
would silently miss. Same guard in `_pickPlaneNode`, where `setPlanes` rebuilds
mid-drag.

**`fitToScene` counts plane nodes.** Its point set is camera positions +
`node_*` in `_skeletonGroup` + **`planeNode_*` in `_planeGroup`** + the origin,
so an annotated plane is not left off screen. The `planeNode_` prefix
deliberately does not match the `node_` test (no double count).

**A fit with no skeleton in it re-fits once when one appears.** `fitToScene`
measures only the CURRENT frame's `node_*` markers; when it counted none it sets
`_refitWhenSkeletonAppears`, and the next `updateSkeleton` that adds a node
marker fits again — once. Anything that moved the view first cancels it: an
OrbitControls `start` (user input only), `animateToCameraPerspective`,
`resetCamera`; skipped while `_viewingCamera` is set. Guarded by
`tests/test-viewport3d-autofit.js`.
- Constructor options `skeletonNodeShape` (`'circle'` sphere / `'square'` cube /
  `'triangle'` tetrahedron / `'x'` crossed bars) and `preserveDrawingBuffer`
  (keeps the WebGL buffer so the canvas can be captured frame-by-frame; Export
  3D Video). A second `Viewport3D` is mounted in the export modals rather than
  duplicating 3D code.
- **`updateSkeleton` updates IN PLACE** (it runs on every playback frame).
  Meshes and materials live in a pool (`_skelPool`, `_ensureSkeletonPool`, one
  slot per instance group from `_newSkeletonSlot`) and are only repositioned /
  recolored; every edge is the ONE shared unit `CylinderGeometry` scaled to
  `(radius, length, radius)`. The pool is rebuilt (`_disposeSkeletonPool`) only
  when node shape, node size, edge weight, show-nodes/edges or the `skeleton`
  object changes. The attached graph equals a fresh build's: one `instance_<g>`
  Group per group with 3D, children = valid `node_*` markers then `edge_*`
  cylinders; unused pooled objects are DETACHED (not hidden), so traversals see
  the same graph. Detaching uses `_detachChildren` (`remove()`), not
  `Object3D.clear()`, so it works with the test runners' THREE mocks. Guarded by
  `tests/test-viewport3d-skeleton-pool.js`. `setEnvironment` still builds per
  call via `_createCylinder`.
- Constructor option / property `skeletonBrightness` (0..1, **default 0.5**)
  scales each group's track/identity color (`multiplyScalar`); part of each
  pooled slot's recolor key (`slot.brightness`), so a change recolors on the
  next `setFrame` without a pool rebuild. The **selected** instance keeps full
  color. Driven by Visibility ▸ 3D Viewer ▸ Brightness (%) (`vis3dBrightness`),
  which `pose/initialization.js`, `export-modals.js` and
  `overlay-export-modal.js` read when constructing their `Viewport3D`.

**Imports from project modules.** `../pose/pose-data.js` —
`points3dNodeCount`, `getPoint3d` (`InstanceGroup.points3d` is a flat
`Float64Array(3N)` with all-NaN triples for missing nodes). No cycle
(`pose-data.js` imports nothing) and no app-state coupling — data arrives via
the options bag. Otherwise uses the global `THREE` from CDN script tags.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `ui/export-modals.js` (Export 3D
Video), `ui/overlay-export-modal.js` (the overlay export's 3D tile).

**User-facing features.** 3D viewport panel — orbit camera, click camera
frustum to fly to that view, "Show Initial View" reset, environment
overlay (skeleton meshes around tracks).

**Coverage.** `tests/e2e/panel-toggle-independence.mjs` pins the pause (ZERO
`renderer.render` calls while collapsed; resync to the CURRENT frame on
re-show). `_rafId === 0` alone is not a sufficient assertion — the loop is
stopped by two independent mechanisms and either one zeroes the handle.

---

### ui/info-tip.js

**Purpose.** The ⓘ explanation, shown where the user is looking — a small
popover beside the pointer. Never `setStatus`: the status bar is the furthest
point on screen from the icon, and it also carries save results and errors.

A **LEAF module** — it imports nothing. Every panel with something to explain is
a caller, and several of those already import each other.

**Key exports.**
- `setInfoTip(el, text)` — declare an explanation. Sets `data-infotip`, and
  **removes `title`** (left on, the native tooltip repeats the sentence a second
  later); the text stays reachable as `aria-label`. Idempotent, so a renderer
  may call it on every repaint.
- `installInfoTips()` — install the delegated listeners; call once at startup
  (`pose/initialization.js`, before any panel renders). Further calls are
  no-ops.
- `hideInfoTip()` — put it away.
- `placeTipNear(el, x, y)` — the placement rule (beside the pointer, flipped at
  the viewport edges) for any `position: fixed` popover. Shared with
  `ui/node-tooltip.js` so the app has one rule for where a tip goes.

**Delegation, not per-element wiring.** One `#infoTip` element and one set of
listeners on `document`, matching `[data-infotip]`. The plane panel rebuilds its
tables on almost every interaction, and a renderer that forgot to re-wire would
fail SILENTLY — an icon with no tip looks like one whose text is empty.

**The tip lives and dies with the hover** — including when the icon was
clicked. A click only guarantees one is showing (what a touch device needs); it
buys no extra time. The one exception is a tip summoned by the KEYBOARD: no
pointer to leave, so it waits for blur or `Esc`.

**A click must not also do the thing underneath.** These icons sit inside a
clickable table row and inside the `<summary>` that folds the section being
asked about, so the click listener is in the CAPTURE phase and calls
`stopPropagation`. Every ⓘ must be a `<button>`: that makes it the click's
activation target, so a `<summary>` ancestor never folds. A plain `<span>` ⓘ
WOULD fold it — activation behaviour runs after dispatch, and stopping
propagation does not reach it. `tests/e2e/plane-section-info.mjs` §4 pins this,
and that the heading BESIDE the icon still folds.

**Four details that are not taste.**
- **The keyboard path is gated on `:focus-visible`.** Clicking a `<button>`
  focuses it, so without the gate a mouse click marked the tip keyboard-held and
  it outlived the pointer. `tests/e2e/info-tip.mjs` §4 goes red without it.
- **`pointer-events: none` on the tip.** It follows the cursor; otherwise it
  takes the hover, fires `mouseout` on the icon and flickers itself away.
- **It FLIPS to the other side of the pointer rather than clamping** — nearly
  every icon is near the right edge, where a clamped tip would cover the pointer.
- **A scroll DISMISSES it** (`position: fixed` does not follow one), unlike the
  pin picker, which repositions: a picker is mid-interaction, a read sentence is
  not.

Leaving the icon, clicking elsewhere, `Esc`, blur, a scroll or a resize all
dismiss.

**Imported by.** `ui/plane-definition.js` (the major sections' ⓘ),
`ui/plane-nodes-panel.js` (the Pinned column's ⓘ), `pose/initialization.js`
(`installInfoTips`), `ui/node-tooltip.js` (`placeTipNear`), `ui/info-panel.js`
(the Ungrouped table's Score / Node header).

Coverage: `tests/e2e/info-tip.mjs` (incl. that the status bar is left untouched
and that an element created after startup is served with no wiring),
`tests/e2e/plane-section-info.mjs`.

### ui/node-tooltip.js

**Purpose.** Rest the pointer on a pose node and its name appears beside it —
and on a prediction, the model's score for THAT node on a second line:
`trunk` / `(score: 0.94)`. SLEAP 1.6.5's node tooltip (`QtNode.setToolTip`,
`sleap/gui/widgets/video.py`), same text: `f"{name}\n(score: {score:.2f})"`, the
name alone for a user node.

**Key exports.** `nodeTooltipText(nodeName, score)` (pure; NaN score → the name
alone), `setNodeTooltipTarget(target, clientX, clientY)` (`target` =
`{key, text, frameIdx, color}` or null; called by `ui/interaction.js` on every mousemove
over a view), `syncNodeTooltipFrame(frameIdx)` (called by `drawAllOverlays` in
`ui/rendering.js`), `hideNodeTooltip()`, `installNodeTooltip()` (once, at
startup), `NODE_TIP_DELAY_MS` (700), `NODE_TIP_AWAKE_MS` (2000).

**Who decides what.** `InteractionManager._nodeTooltipTarget` turns the hover's
hit (`findNearestNode` / `findNearestUnlinkedNode`) into the target: an edge hit,
a plane node and empty canvas give none, a reprojection is named without a
score, and the score is `Session.predictedNodeScore` — so the tooltip never
decides whether a number is a prediction's. This module only times, places and
hides it.

**Timing is Qt's.** The first tip waits `NODE_TIP_DELAY_MS`; while one is
showing, and for `NODE_TIP_AWAKE_MS` after it hides, the next node's shows at
once — an instant tip would flicker over the points being placed. A press, a key
or the wheel hides it and restores the delay. A shown tip stays where it
appeared.

**It never outlives what it describes.** A new frame moves the node from under a
still pointer, so `drawAllOverlays` calls `syncNodeTooltipFrame` (a redraw
naming no frame — `ui/plane-angle.js` calls `drawAllOverlays()` bare — is not a
frame change). Leaving the view, a press, a key, the wheel, a scroll, a resize
and window blur hide it too. It holds strings and numbers only, never an
Instance, so it cannot keep a lazy frame alive.

**The color says WHICH skeleton.** Where skeletons overlap the text does not say
whose node it is, so the tip's left edge is a 5 px stripe in the color the
hovered instance is drawn FROM (`target.color` → `--node-tip-color`): its
track's, or its identity's in Identity mode, darkened like the skeleton for a
second ungrouped instance on one track — the base color, not a selection's white
or a prediction's tint. The interaction manager takes it from the renderer's own
functions (`ui/overlays.js`), so it cannot name a color that is not on screen.

It reuses the ⓘ tip's look (`.info-tip.node-tip`; `.node-tip` adds
`white-space: pre-line` and the stripe) and placement (`placeTipNear`), and is
`pointer-events: none` — a tip that took the hover would make the view see a
`mouseleave` and hide it.

**Imports from project modules.** `./info-tip.js` — `placeTipNear`.

**Imported by.** `ui/interaction.js`, `ui/rendering.js`, `pose/initialization.js`.

Coverage: `tests/test-node-scores.mjs` (the text) and
`tests/e2e/node-score-tooltip.mjs` (real mouse: timing, scores, placement,
hiding, and the stripe across tracks / identities / overlaps).

### ui/skeleton-edit-warning.js

**Purpose.** The confirmation the Skeleton tab's five edits — add node, remove
node, rename node, add edge, remove edge — go through before they are applied.
The skeleton is the shape every annotation is stored against (`Instance` keeps
one flat `Float64Array(2N)` keyed by node INDEX) and LUCID is one skeleton per
project, so a node typed into that box re-shapes every instance in every loaded
session at once.

Six things about it:
- **It is a title, ONE SENTENCE, and the counts.** No bulleted `What changes`
  block and no always-on `There is no undo…` caution — prose above the counts
  becomes scenery and costs the lazy warning its weight.
  `tests/e2e/skeleton-edit-warning.mjs` asserts both are ABSENT.
- **It states a TOTAL, then WHICH session.** The headline block is the whole
  project, folded from `perSession` (`pose/skeleton-edit-impact.js`). A
  `By session` block appears when more than one session is loaded, with **no
  `Total` row** — two copies of one number invite the reader to check them
  against each other. The test asserts its absence.
- **A lazy project gets a RED warning advising against the edit**, naming the
  non-resident frame count. Propagation is resident-only by necessity, so those
  frames come back on the PREVIOUS skeleton silently, until a save writes two
  node counts into one project. A fully-resident project gets no such warning.
- **It is SKIPPED when there is nothing to warn about**
  (`skeletonEditNeedsConfirmation`) — building the first skeleton would
  otherwise be a modal per node.
- **"Do not show again"** is cached in `localStorage.skeletonEditWarningOff` —
  browser-local, must NOT reach the `.slp` (opening a colleague's project must
  not silence their warnings). Three rules:
  - **The suppression check runs FIRST, before the tally**, which walks a lazy
    project's whole columnar store.
  - **It is recorded only when the edit is APPLIED.** Ticked and then cancelled
    does nothing: guessing which intention won would silence a data-loss warning
    on the strength of a dialog the user rejected.
  - **Storage failures fall back to WARNING**, never to skipping.
  The way back on is `#skeletonWarnOffNote` in the Skeleton tab (shown only
  while set; wired by `setupSkeletonEditing`, displayed by
  `populateSkeletonTable`) — a setting a user can switch off and never find
  again is a trap.
- **The caller owns the mutation.** This module shows a dialog and calls back;
  each edit's propagation differs, so it stays in `ui/info-panel.js` beside its
  control.

**Key exports.**
- `confirmSkeletonEdit(edit, onConfirm)` — `onConfirm` is called with the
  sessions the edit applies to (those on the open session's skeleton), in every
  path including the two that never open the dialog. The split is taken BEFORE
  the callback mutates, which is the only moment it can be. `edit` is
  `{kind: 'add-node'|'remove-node'|'rename-node'|'add-edge'|'remove-edge', label}`.
- `isSkeletonEditWarningSuppressed()` / `setSkeletonEditWarningSuppressed(off)` —
  the "Do not show again" preference, read and cleared by `ui/info-panel.js`'s
  Skeleton-tab note.
- `describeSkeletonEdit(edit)` → `{title, lead}`. One table rather than strings
  at five call sites, so a consequence cannot drift from what the code does. The
  three non-destructive edits open on *"No coordinates move"* — a dialog that
  only ever lists damage trains the user to dismiss it.

**Geometry and classes are shared with `ui/origin-rebase.js`'s confirmation**
(`.origin-rebase-block` / `-table` / `-caution`, plus `.skeleton-edit-modal` on
the same `max-height` + sticky-actions rule). Its own additions are the action
row (`.skeleton-edit-actions` / `-buttons` / `-again`, checkbox on the LEFT —
it qualifies the action, it is not a third one) and `#skeletonWarnOffNote`.

**Imports from project modules.** `./app-state.js` (`state`),
`../pose/skeleton-edit-impact.js`.

**Imported by.** `ui/info-panel.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/skeleton-edit-warning.mjs` — all five edits in the real
app over a two-session project, incl. Esc/Cancel leaving it byte-identical, the
lazy warning with a resident negative control, and the no-dialog gate.

### ui/recording-info.js

**Purpose.** The Session tab's **Recording** section (`#recordingInfoSection`,
a `<details>` under Session Info): a campy recording's `session.campyRecording`
(`loading/campy-session.js`). Hidden for any other session. Rows: status (the
warning colour unless `complete`, with a plain explanation), experiment, animal
IDs (with an ⓘ saying they are not LUC3D's identities), notes, other fields,
annotations with their times, frame rate (and what the video says when it
differs by > 1%), start time, campy's version, the calibration check ("same as
campy-live's", or a warning naming both hashes), cameras in only one of
session.json and the calibration, and a Camera / Serial / Video table.

**Key exports.** `setupRecordingInfo()` (once at init: `persistSectionState`,
browser-local), `renderRecordingInfo(session)` (called by `updateInfoPanel`),
`formatCampyTime`, `RECORDING_SECTION_ID`, `ANIMAL_IDS_INFO`.

**Dependencies.** `loading/campy-session.js`, `ui/section-state.js`,
`ui/info-tip.js`, `ICON_INFO` from `ui/plane-definition.js` (read only inside a
function, so the cycle through the info panel is hoist-safe).

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/campy-session-folder.mjs`.

### ui/triangulation-methods-figure.js

**Purpose.** The Settings ▸ Default Triangulation choice: two cards that each
explain one method (DLT, Refined = bundle adjustment) and are clicked to pick
it (`role="radio"`, `data-method` `'dlt'`/`'ba'`; `ui/settings-modal.js` wires
the selection). Two top-down sketches of the
same three cameras: each camera's 2D detection on its image plane, its line of
sight, the 3D answer, where that answer lands back in each camera and the pixel
error between the two. One camera's detection is a stray: DLT is pulled toward
it and every camera ends up a little off; Refined starts at the DLT point and
fits the two cameras that agree; in this layout that ignores the stray camera
entirely, so it gets no line, landing spot or error (a `null` landing). Captions are HTML
so they wrap; the sketches are inline SVG coloured by theme variables
(`.tri-methods` in `styles.css`).

**The coordinates are an illustration, not solver output.** They were computed
once for this layout (an equal-weight least-squares point for DLT, a robust
pixel-error minimum for Refined) and are hard-coded; the real solvers are
`triangulatePointDLT` / `triangulatePointBA` (`pose/triangulation-core.js`).

**Don't call Refined more accurate in general.** Measured on held-out cameras
(9 proofread SLAP sessions), shipped Refined beat DLT in only 1 of 9 (median
3.32 vs 3.20 px): it wins when one view is clearly wrong, but its L1 polish
step leaves everyday points slightly worse. Its "never worse than DLT" guard is
on the in-sample reprojection error only. The caption (and the toolbar's Ref
tooltip in `index.html`) say just that.

**Key exports.** `buildTriangulationMethodsFigure()` → a `<figure>`.

**Imports.** None. **Imported by** `ui/settings-modal.js`.

### ui/smoothing-button.js

**Purpose.** The toolbar's Temporal Smoothing button state (luc3d #134):
`#tbSmooth` is blue (`.active`) while any 3D in the session is smoothed. Its
menu lights (`.applied`) only the scope of the LAST run, never both: **All
frames** after an all-frames run (while anything is still smoothed), **This
frame** after a this-frame run, on the frames it smoothed. The toggle
(`#tbSmoothToggle`) reads **Remove smoothing** while something is smoothed,
**Restore smoothing** while a removal is kept (`hasRemovedSmoothing`), and is
disabled otherwise; its tooltip follows. Never while it carries
`.plane-mode-locked` (that lock owns `disabled` and the tooltip then; the next
draw re-derives them after the unlock's snapshot restore).

**Key exports.** `syncSmoothingButton()` — re-derives that state from the
session. `noteSmoothingScope(session, 'all' | 'frame', config)` — records the
scope and resolved windows a run used, kept by
`import-export/smoothing-metadata.js` (`noteSmoothingRun` / `smoothingRunOf`),
which saves them so a reopened project lights the same item. Smoothing lapses by itself when a re-solve or edit replaces a group's
3D (`isPoints3dSmoothed` keys the array), so nothing tracks it: `ui/rendering.js`
calls this on every overlay draw and the pass calls it after running.
`hasSmoothed3d` (`pose/temporal-smoothing.js`) keeps the per-draw cost small.

**Imports.** `./app-state.js` (`state`), `../pose/temporal-smoothing.js`
(`hasSmoothed3d`, `hasRemovedSmoothing`), `../import-export/smoothing-metadata.js` (`noteSmoothingRun`,
`smoothingRunOf`). **Imported by** `ui/rendering.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`.
Covered by `tests/e2e/temporal-smoothing-display.mjs` §E–H and
`tests/e2e/smoothing-save-reopen.mjs`.

### ui/run-buttons.js

**Purpose.** The toolbar's Track (`#tbTrackAll`) and Triangulate
(`#tbTriangulateAll`) buttons are blue (`.active`) once that step has been run
on the active session and while its result stands (`pose/run-state.js`), as
Smooth is while smoothing is applied (`ui/smoothing-button.js`). Derived on
every overlay draw, so session switches and results that go away (re-tracking
replaces Triangulate's 3D) need no bookkeeping.

**Exports.** `syncRunButtons()`. **Imports.** `./app-state.js` (`state`),
`../pose/run-state.js` (`hasTrackResult`, `hasTriangulateResult`).
**Imported by** `ui/rendering.js` (every `drawAllOverlays`). **Tests.**
`tests/e2e/run-buttons-blue.mjs`.

### ui/section-state.js

**Purpose.** Remember which collapsible `<details>` sections a user left open,
across page loads — **loading a project reloads the page**, so without this the
markup's `open` attributes win. State lives in `localStorage`.

**Browser-local display taste, NOT project state** — it must never be written
into the `.slp`: it would move `save-golden-digest.mjs`, and opening a
colleague's project would refold your panel.

A **LEAF module** — it imports nothing. `ui/info-panel.js` and
`ui/plane-definition.js` both use it and sit on opposite sides of several import
chains, so any import here would risk closing a cycle.

**Key exports.**
- `persistSectionState(id, key)` — restore one `<details>` from `key`'s map,
  then listen for `toggle`. **Restore first, listen second**: assigning
  `el.open` fires `toggle`. An id with **no stored value keeps the markup
  default**, so a section can ship collapsed.
- `persistSectionStates(ids, key)` — the same for several under one key.

**Storage is best-effort on BOTH sides** (private windows / blocked site data
throw on read and write); a failure degrades to the markup default. The `toggle`
handler **re-reads the map** rather than mutating a captured copy: several
sections share one key, so a stale copy would write back its neighbours' old
state.

**Callers and their keys.**
- `skeletonSectionsOpen` — `skeletonNodesSection`, `skeletonEdgesSection`.
- `planeSectionsOpen` — `planeNodesDetails`, `planePlanesDetails`,
  `planeMembersDetails`, `planeEdgesDetails`, `planeActionsDetails`,
  `planeAppearanceDetails`, `meshObjectsDetails`, `originResultDetails`.
  **`originDangerDetails` is deliberately ABSENT** — it ships collapsed (it holds
  the actions that rewrite the calibration) and must not reopen because it was
  expanded once in another project.

**Imported by.** `ui/info-panel.js`, `ui/plane-definition.js`.

**The panel's MAJOR sections are OUTLINED, its sub-sections are not** (styling,
documented here). `.plane-panel > .info-section > .plane-details` has its
existing 1px border recoloured to the accent, all four sides — it is where a
tall open section ENDS that is ambiguous — rather than adding a second outline,
which would cost width in a ~300px panel. Danger Zone is outlined in the error
colour, restated at this selector's specificity because `.plane-panel >
.info-section > ...` would otherwise win.

Coverage: `tests/e2e/plane-section-state.mjs` (reload round trip, a repaint not
reopening a collapsed section, the Danger Zone control, nothing reaching the
project or dirty flag, a throwing `localStorage`).

### ui/lazy-select.js

**Purpose.** A `<select>` that builds its full option list only when the user
reaches for it — the info panel's per-row Track dropdown, which `updateFrameInfo`
rebuilds on every update (see `ui/info-panel.js` ▸ "The Track `<select>` is
built LAZILY").

A **LEAF module** — it imports nothing — so the browser suite bridges it
(`window.__LazySelect`) without loading the app.

**Key exports.**
- `buildLazySelect({ head, tail, value, label, entries, cssText })` — returns a
  select holding `head`, the entry `[value, label]`, and `tail`, with `value`
  selected. The middle option is omitted when `value` is the head's or the
  tail's, or when `label` is `undefined` (no entry has that value, so nothing is
  selected, as an eager select would show). On the first `mousedown` or `focus`
  it calls `entries()` once and inserts every `[value, label]` between head and
  tail, keeping the selection.

Three things about it:
- **`mousedown` AND `focus`** both fire before the list opens or a key acts, so
  the user always picks from the complete list. Script that assigns `.value`
  without either selects nothing; dispatch `focus` first.
- **Filling locks the closed width** (`style.width = offsetWidth`), so a long
  track name does not widen the select under the pointer.
- **The caller supplies `label`**, so finding it never builds the list.

**Imported by.** `ui/info-panel.js`.

Coverage: `tests/test-lazy-select.js`, `tests/e2e/info-panel-many-tracks.mjs`.

### ui/panel-visibility.js

**Purpose.** The single answer to "is the 3D viewport / info panel actually on
screen?", plus the deferred-refresh bookkeeping that makes skipping work while
hidden safe. Hiding a panel is how the user asks for its work to stop, so the
collapse state has to be readable by code, not just CSS.

**Key exports.**
- `isViewport3DVisible()` / `isInfoPanelVisible()` — read `#viewport3dContainer`
  / `#infoPanelWrapper`. The **DOM is the source of truth** (no mirrored
  boolean). A `collapsed` class, or `display:none` (the 3D container's
  Three.js-init-failure state), means hidden. A **missing element means
  visible**: the unit-test runner has no app chrome, and skipping every refresh
  there would hide test failures.
- `markInfoPanelStale()` / `consumeInfoPanelStale()` — read-and-clear flag.
  Every info-panel populate function is a stateless full rebuild, so one call on
  re-show catches up; the flag avoids a redundant rebuild when nothing went
  stale (`populateVideosTable` walks every frame).
- `markViewport3DSkipped()`, `skipped` (`{ infoPanel, viewport3d }` counters,
  also on `window.__lucidPanelVis`) — diagnostics read by
  `tests/e2e/panel-toggle-independence.mjs`; a gate that still does the work has
  no visual signature.
- `collapseViewport3D(viewport3d)` — collapse the 3D panel if expanded (returns
  whether it did): park the inline width, add `collapsed`,
  `viewport3d.setVisible(false)`. The collapse half of `toggle3DViewport`; it
  lives here so `pose/tracker.js` can close the panel after Track All without
  importing `ui/ui-wiring.js` (an import loop). The viewport is a PARAMETER to
  keep this module a leaf.

**Imports from project modules.** **None — a leaf module by design**; its
importers already import each other, so any import here would close a cycle.

**Imported by.** `ui/info-panel.js` (gates `updateInfoPanel` /
`updateFrameInfo`), `ui/ui-wiring.js` (`refreshInfoPanelAfterShow`,
`toggle3DViewport`), `pose/initialization.js` (gates `update3DViewport` and
`setup3DViewport`), `pose/tracker.js` (`collapseViewport3D` after Track All).

**Note.** `ui/viewport3d.js` deliberately does NOT import this — it takes a
per-instance `visible` flag, because the export modals' own `Viewport3D`
instances must render regardless of the main panel.

**User-facing features.** Hiding the 3D viewport (`\`) or info panel (`I`)
actually stops the corresponding rendering / data work.

---

## loading/

### loading/frame-worker.js

**Purpose.** Worker that uses `SLPPackageReader` + h5wasm-lazy-files to
extract embedded video frames from `.pkg.slp` files via HTTP range
requests. Spawned by `import-export/slp-import.js` (twice — for two
loading paths). Module-typed worker.

**Message protocol.**
- IN: `{type: 'loadUrl', url}` / `{type: 'loadFile', file}` — open SLP
  package.
- IN: `{type: 'getVideos'}` — list embedded videos.
- IN: `{type: 'getFrame', videoKey, embeddedIdx}` — extract one frame.
- IN: `{type: 'findFrame', videoKey, displayFrame}` — find embedded
  index for a display frame.
- IN: `{type: 'close'}`.
- OUT: `{type: 'ready'}`, `{type: 'log', message, level}`,
  `{type: 'videos', videos}`, `{type: 'frame', bytes, format, ...}`,
  `{type: 'error', error}`.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./slp-package-reader.js` — `SLPPackageReader`.
- `../lib/h5wasm/hdf5_hl.js` (vendored h5wasm ESM).

**Imported by.** Spawned via `new Worker(new URL('../loading/frame-worker.js',
import.meta.url), {type: 'module'})` from `import-export/slp-import.js`
(two call sites). Its `Worker` is driven by `EmbeddedVideoDecoder`
(`loading/video.js`).

**User-facing features.** Loading `.pkg.slp` projects with embedded video
frames (off-main-thread to keep UI responsive).

---

### loading/percam-slp-choice.js

**Purpose.** The rule for which `.slp` a camera directory is loaded from, as a
pure function. Imports **nothing** — no project modules, no DOM — so it bridges
into `tests/test-runner.html` (session-loader pulls app.js through its import
graph and cannot be loaded there).

**Key export.** `chooseCameraSlp(slps)` → `{file, version, newer}`. See the
`loading/session-loader.js` entry for the rule and why `lastModified` is
deliberately NOT authoritative. `newer` is the most-recently-modified candidate
when that is not the chosen file, else `null` — the signal that a leftover
`_vN` is outranking a file the user just wrote.

**Imports from project modules.** None (deliberately).

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js`, which re-exports it so its own
import site is unchanged; bridged into the test runner as
`window.__PerCamSlpChoice`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-percam-slp-choice.js`.

---

### loading/calibration-pick.js

**Purpose.** Two small facts about calibration FILES that several modules need
and that must not disagree: what the re-based calibration is called, and which
file to load when a folder holds more than one candidate.

**No imports, deliberately.** `loading/session-loader.js` (CDN Three.js) and
`import-export/file-io.js` cannot be loaded by a Node test, and the selection
rule wants a unit test. It is also outside the `ui/origin-rebase.js` /
`ui/origin-definition.js` cycle, where a `const` exported from either sits in
TDZ for whichever side initializes second.

**Key exports.**
- `REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME` — `'calibration-rebased.toml'`, the file BOTH
  origin actions write (byte-identical TOML for a given origin). Deliberately not
  `calibration.toml`: that one is usually shared with tools outside LUCID.
- `pickCalibrationFile(matches)` → `{file, ambiguous}`. Prefers
  `REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME` (case-insensitively), else the lexicographically
  first match, so the choice is DETERMINISTIC across loads of the same folder.
  `ambiguous` is the NAMES of the candidates passed over, so a caller can say
  what it ignored. Does not mutate its argument: the loaders keep scanning.

**Why it exists.** With two matches, last-scanned-wins depends on an
enumeration order neither the File System Access API nor `webkitdirectory`
promises, and picking the stale file is silent: it parses, every camera loads,
and every reprojection is wrong.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js` (both folder loaders),
`ui/origin-rebase.js`, `ui/origin-definition.js`. (`loading/video-file-pick.js`
only cites it in a comment.)

**Tests.** `tests/test-calibration-file-pick.mjs`.

### loading/campy-session.js

**Purpose.** What a campy recording folder says about itself. campy (2.1.1+)
writes `<recording>/session.json` (schema `campy-session/1`); campy-live writes
`campy-live-session*.json` beside it (schema `campy-live-session/1`). Fields are
documented in campy's `docs/recordings.md`. This module finds the manifests
among a picked folder's files and turns them into the record the loaders put on
`session.campyRecording`. **Read only, never written to the `.slp`** (the folder
is the source; picking it again reads it again; `save-golden-digest.mjs` does
not move). A folder without session.json gives null everywhere, so every loader
behaves exactly as before.

**No imports**, so `tests/test-campy-session.mjs` loads it in Node.

**Key exports.**
- `findCampyManifestFiles(files)` → `{session, live, root}|null`: the
  SHALLOWEST `session.json`, the `campy-live-session*.json` in the same
  directory, and that directory's relative path (what campy's relative `video`
  paths join to).
- `parseCampySession(text)` → the record, or null for anything but
  `campy-session/1`: `frameRate` (`config.effective.frameRate.value`, null when
  absent or ≤ 0), `status`, `startedAt`, `campyVersion`, `experiment` (`name`,
  `animalIds` as strings, `notes`, `fields` as `[key, value]`), `annotations`,
  `cameras` sorted by campy's `index` (`name`, `serial`, `folder`, `video`),
  `failures`, `error`; plus `live`, `calibrationCheck`, `cameraMismatch`,
  `videoFrameRate`, filled by the loader. campy's `"None"` for an unset field is
  no value (`campyUnset`). **Animal IDs are display only**: campy-live numbers
  animals as it first matches them, so they must never name identities.
- `parseCampyLiveSession(text, fileName)` → `{file, calibration: {path, sha256,
  cameraNames}|null}` (null calibration = a 2D-only run).
- `parseCampyJson(text)` — both parsers read through it: `JSON.parse`, and on
  failure the text again with Python's bare `NaN` / `Infinity` / `-Infinity`
  (outside strings) as null. campy writes its manifests with Python's json
  module, and real campy-live manifests hold `Infinity` (`--wait-sec`'s
  default), which strict `JSON.parse` rejects; skipping the file silently
  turned the calibration check off. A manifest that still cannot be read is
  skipped with a console warning.
- `readCampyRecording(files, readText?)` — both, as one record (`.root`, `.live`).
- `campyCameraFor`, `orderByCampyCameras(record, items, keyOf)` (campy order,
  others after in their own order, stable), `campyVideoFile` (matched by full
  relative path), `preferCampyVideo`.
- `compareCampyCameraNames(record, calibrationNames)`,
  `sha256Hex(buffer)`, `compareCalibrationSha256(record, sha, fileName)` (a
  match with ANY campy-live run counts), `frameRatesDisagree` (> 1%).
- `describeCampyStatus(record)` → `{ok, label, detail}` in plain words;
  `campyRecordingWarnings(record, {status})`, `withCampyWarnings(message, kind,
  record, opts)` — a load's final status-bar message with the warnings added
  (`success` → `warning`). `calibrationMismatchText`, `pathBaseName`.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js`, `import-export/slp-import.js`,
`ui/recording-info.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-campy-session.mjs` (fixture
`tests/fixtures/campy-recording/`, a real session.json),
`tests/e2e/campy-session-folder.mjs`.

### loading/video-file-pick.js

**Purpose.** Which of a camera's candidate videos is the session recording.
Two rules about calibration-named files that four load paths need and that must
not disagree.

**No imports, deliberately** — same reason as `loading/calibration-pick.js`:
this decision is pure and worth pinning in Node.

**Key exports.**
- `isCalibrationImagesVideo(file)` — the HARD exclusion. True for a clip under a
  `calibration_images/` path segment at ANY depth (so the answer does not depend
  on the picked root). `session-loader.js`'s `isCalibrationVideoFile` is just
  this.
- `hasCalibrationStem(file)` — the SOFT signal: a stem ending in `-calibration`
  or `_calibration`. Positional — `calibration-cam1.mp4` is not a match.
- `preferNonCalibrationVideos(files, groupKeyFn)` → `{kept, dropped}`. Drops each
  calibration-STEMMED video that has a non-calibration sibling under the same
  group key. Order-preserving, non-mutating, and a file whose key is null is
  never dropped.
- `matchVideoToCamera(file, cameraNames, refBaseByCam)` — the camera a video
  belongs to, by parent directory, then the camera name ANYWHERE in the stem,
  then the filename the project references for that camera. Case-insensitive;
  null when nothing matches. Used both to assign a camera and, in the same load,
  as the group key above — matching twice with two rules is how a video gets
  dropped for one camera and bound to another.

**Why the stem is only a hint.** As a hard exclusion it dropped ordinary
session videos like `cam1-calibration.mp4`, leaving a folder load with ZERO views
and no message; real calibration clips live under `calibration_images/`.

**The grouping is per CAMERA, not per folder** — a folder-wide rule would let
one camera's plain video suppress another camera's only video.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js` (the per-camera folder loader, the
single-SLP folder loader, and `attachVideosForLazyReopen`),
`import-export/slp-import.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-video-file-pick.mjs`,
`tests/e2e/calibration-named-videos-load.mjs` (both real loaders).

### loading/track-union.js

**Purpose.** What `session.tracks` IS for a per-camera session folder, where
every camera's `.slp`/`.h5` carries its own track list, as one pure rule shared
by all three per-camera paths: the eager loop in
`handleLoadSessionFolderPerCamera`, `SioLazyLoader` and `LazyFrameLoader`.

**The decision.** `session.tracks` is **the union of the cameras' track NAMES,
cameras taken in sorted camera-name order, each camera's names in its file
order**, and every camera's own track index is re-expressed as an index into
it. Per-camera lists were rejected because every consumer of a trackIdx indexes
ONE list — overlays (names, `getTrackColor`), the Track select, the Tracks
Timeline, the export modal's track stats and Custom Delete's track filter (both
aggregate across cameras), `remapTracksFromIdentity` and the streaming writer —
so each would need a camera argument. Concatenation was rejected: it multiplies
the list with duplicate names and makes `metadata.lucid.hiddenTracks` (saved by
NAME) ambiguous. A name two cameras share becomes one session track (one index,
colour, Track-select entry); that says nothing about the animal, and nothing
needs it to: the cross-view tracker keys association, `trustTracks` votes and
`frameIdentityMap` by **(camera, trackIdx)**, which any per-camera-injective map
preserves.

**Why it exists.** Taking the list from whichever camera's file opened first
(the lazy loaders open in parallel) made `session.tracks` depend on I/O timing
and left other cameras with out-of-range or misnamed track indices.

**Key exports.**
- `unionTrackNames(perCamera)` → `{names, remapByCam}` for
  `[{camName, names}]`. Input order is irrelevant. Names are matched as a
  MULTISET (the k-th `x` of a camera maps to the k-th `x` slot), so a camera
  that repeats a name keeps distinct tracks and every per-camera map is
  injective. Camera names compare by UTF-16 code unit (`<`), so the order is
  locale-independent.
- `remapTrackIdx(remap, ownIdx)` → session index or `-1`. Trackless stays
  trackless, and so does an index OUTSIDE the camera's own list (as sleap-io's
  lazy materializer treats it); keeping it raw would name whatever session track
  sits there. Accepts an `Int32Array` or a plain object map.
- `isIdentityRemap(remap)`.

**Imports from project modules.** None (deliberately) — loads in Node.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js` (the eager per-camera loop and
`addColumnarFramesToSession`), `loading/sio-lazy-loader.js` (`_unifyTracks`),
`pose/triangulation.js` (`LazyFrameLoader._unifyTracks`,
`denseOccupancyToSparse`, `_remapFrameTracks`).

**Tests.** `tests/test-track-union.mjs`, `tests/test-lazy-track-union.js`
(`LazyFrameLoader`, all six metadata orders), `tests/e2e/percam-track-union.mjs`
(the real folder loader, lazy in all six open orders plus eager).

### loading/session-loader.js

**Purpose.** Orchestrator for every session-loading workflow — empty
session, per-camera SLPs, single-SLP, multi-session, video-only,
calibration-only. Owns view/grid layout, video selection prompts,
filesystem enumeration, decoder rebuild.

**Key exports.**
- Loaders: `handleLoadCalibration`, `handleLoadVideos`,
  `handleLoadMultiSession`, `loadSingleSessionFromCache`,
  `handleLoadSessionFolder`, `handleEmptySession`,
  `handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp`,
  `handleLoadSessionFolderPerCamera`, `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`,
  `attachVideosForLazyReopen`. Also `addColumnarFramesToSession` (the
  per-camera load's Instance builder from the SLP worker's columnar result;
  exported for `tests/e2e/slp-import-fast-compound.mjs`).
  `handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp()` loads a folder holding a project `.slp`
  plus `videos/` + calibration. It reads the SLP with the **typed** reader
  (`parseSlpViaSleapIO`, raw `parseSlpH5` only as fallback) and restores
  grouping, `nulledNodes`/occlusion, identities and 3D via the shared
  `restoreGroupingAndUnlink` (`import-export/slp-import.js`), then the rest of
  `metadata.lucid` through `readVisibilityMetadata`, `readSmoothingMetadata`
  and `readPlaneMetadata`.
  Unlike the other readers it **adds** a session (it is also the Sessions list's
  "Add session folder"), so it calls `resetPlaneState()` only when no session is
  open. With a project open its planes stay, and the new session's 2D is seated
  on them by node ID only if `planeMetadataConflict` finds the file's nodes and
  planes in the open model under the same IDs and names; otherwise the 2D is
  dropped and the status bar says so (plane-node IDs restart at 1 in every
  project). Covered by `tests/e2e/reopen-folder-session-settings.mjs`. Views are
  ordered by **calibration camera index**, not enumeration order, so panes don't
  reshuffle on reload.
  `handleEmptySession()` creates a blank, video-less session and makes it
  active (the session-strip **"+"** button). It **inherits the shared project
  skeleton** via `buildRememberedSkeleton()` (one skeleton per project); only
  the very first session mints a fresh `Skeleton` and registers it with
  `setProjectSkeleton`.
- Video assignment: `autoAssignVideosToCameras`, `forceVideoSelection`,
  `forceVideoSelectionWithFolder`, `matchSessionFolder`,
  `pickParentDirectoryForSessions`, `showParentDirMatchSummary`.
  `forceVideoSelectionWithFolder(refInfo, sessionName, options)` accepts
  `options.allowSkip` (a "Skip — Load Videos Later" button; used by the lazy
  reopen) and closes on `Esc`; both resolve `null`, which every caller treats as
  "no videos picked".
  `forceVideoSelection(refInfo, options)` (Load Project on a V2/legacy `.json`,
  Add SLP with no embedded video) is NOT blocking: a **Continue Without Video**
  button and `Esc` both resolve `[]`, which both callers treat as "no videos"
  (#337); a cancelled picker leaves it up. `options.title` names what was
  loaded, and the "Expected:" line is omitted when `refInfo` is empty — callers
  pass `''`, not a `'(unknown)'` placeholder.
- `adoptLabeledFrameCount()` — sizes a labeled project that has NO view:
  `totalFrames = last labeled frame + 1`, plus the readout and the timeline;
  returns the first labeled frame (or -1 when it did nothing). Called right
  after `updateTotalFrames()`, whose no-decoder branch resets the count to 0 on
  purpose (so one session's length cannot leak into another) — which is why this
  is a separate step rather than a change to `updateTotalFrames`, whose many
  callers rely on that reset. Callers: `handleLoadProject` (a load continued
  without video) and `handleLoadVideos` (a Load Videos where nothing decoded).
  A no-op with any view, and on a LAZY session, whose resident `frameGroups`
  would give a plausible, too-small count.
- `isCalibrationVideoFile(file)` — a thin wrapper of `isCalibrationImagesVideo`
  (`loading/video-file-pick.js`): true for clips under a `calibration_images/`
  path segment. The folder scans recurse into camera subfolders and those
  filenames embed the camera name, so they would otherwise be matched to a
  camera. Applied in the parent-directory pick (FSA + webkitdirectory), the
  "Select Session Folder" scan and the SLP-import video filter. A
  `-calibration` / `_calibration` stem is NOT excluded, only de-prioritized by
  `preferNonCalibrationVideos` (per-camera loader, single-SLP loader,
  `attachVideosForLazyReopen`, SLP import).
- View/grid: `createViewForVideoFile`, `removeVideoFile`, `updateGridLayout`,
  `createVideoPromptCell`, `fitCanvasesToCells`, `cellResizeObserver`,
  `reapplyAllZoom`, `rebuildVideoController`, `updateTotalFrames`,
  `adoptLabeledFrameCount` (see Video assignment above).
  `reapplyAllZoom()` runs `videoController.reapplyZoom` on every view — after
  `fitCanvasesToCells` in `cellResizeObserver`, and on a devicePixelRatio change
  via a module-level `matchMedia('(resolution: Ndppx)')` watcher (re-armed each
  change): a view at native resolution is scaled in DEVICE pixels, and moving the
  window to another-DPR display fires no ResizeObserver. Headless DPR emulation
  fires neither, so `tests/e2e/native-resolution-zoom.mjs` calls it itself.
  `removeVideoFile(videoFile)` is `createViewForVideoFile`'s inverse and the
  whole of the Videos tab's **Remove Video**: closes the dock pane, drops the
  view from `state.views` (taking the view-strip thumbnail), splices
  `state.videoFiles`, **remaps every session's `videoFileIndices`** across the
  splice (they index `state.videoFiles`; a stale one re-points a session at its
  neighbour's video), closes the decoder and removes it from
  `state.decoderPool`/`_decoderPoolCold`, then resettles everything derived from
  the view list. The session's CAMERA is kept — it carries calibration and
  annotations. Accepts a `{name, assignedCamera}` descriptor too, since the
  Videos table synthesises rows from `state.views` when `state.videoFiles` is
  empty. **It re-seats `state.singleViewIndex` BY NAME, not by clamping** —
  removing a video BEFORE the solo'd one slides the next camera into its slot;
  removing the solo'd view itself falls back to the clamp AND re-renders the
  dock (`updateVideoGridDisplay`). Covered by `tests/e2e/videos-panel-buttons.mjs`.
- Session-mode UI: `showSessionModeModal`, `showMissingFilesPopup`.
- Filesystem: `enumerateDirectoryHandle`.
- **campy recordings** (`loading/campy-session.js`):
  `adoptCampyRecording(session, files, {record, calibFile, calibrationNames})`
  reads a picked folder's session.json (+ campy-live's manifests) onto
  `session.campyRecording` (in memory only; null and untouched without one) and
  runs `checkCampyCalibration(session, calibFile)` (SHA-256 through
  `readBlobArrayBuffer`, compared with campy-live's). Called by the per-camera
  loader, the single-SLP loader, `attachVideosForLazyReopen`,
  `handleLoadSlpFile`'s Select Session Folder path, and `handleLoadCalibration`
  (which reports only on the calibration). With a record:
  `updateTotalFrames` uses its `frameRate` over the video estimate (and sets the
  fps readout's `title`; the estimate is kept as `videoFrameRate` when it
  differs by > 1%); views follow campy's camera order (the calibration's own
  camera list keeps its order); each camera loads the file at campy's `video`
  path; with no calibration, views are named after campy's cameras (as every
  uncalibrated camera folder's view is, never by video stem — campy's are all
  `0.mp4`); the single-SLP loader takes videos from
  session.json when there is no `videos/` and reads the root `.slp` over
  campy-live-export's `points3d.h5`; the load's final status gains the
  warnings (`withCampyWarnings`). `forceVideoSelectionWithFolder`'s folder
  button and `pickParentDirectoryForSessions` pass the root-level manifests
  through with the videos (JSON, so every caller's video filter drops them).
- `recomputeUploadedCameras`.
- Misc re-exports: `resolveImportTrackIdx` (from
  `import-export/import-track-resolve.js`) and `chooseCameraSlp` (from
  `./percam-slp-choice.js`) — both live outside this module so they are
  unit-testable; session-loader pulls app.js and can't be bridged into the test
  runner.

`rebuildVideoController()` reports, after every load/session-switch, views with
a real decoder but no `_mbBackend`: `setStatus('N of M camera(s) fell back to
HTML5 seeking...', 'warning')`. A decoder that dropped its backend ON PURPOSE
because WebCodecs cannot decode the codec (`decoder._mbUnavailable.reason ===
'codec'` — Firefox + HEVC, see `loading/video.js`) gets its own clause (`… step
with <video> seeks: this browser cannot decode HEVC with WebCodecs …`). Both
clauses can appear, joined by `; `.

Fresh-session creation sites (video-only, calibration-only, multi-cam directory)
seed the skeleton from `buildRememberedSkeleton()` (else empty); SLP/project
load paths parse their own embedded skeleton via `parseSkeletonJSON`.

`handleLoadVideos` uses `paneManager.addAllViewsAsGrid()` only on the **first**
load; later loads add just the new views via the dedup-aware
`paneManager.addVideoPanel(name, { direction: 'right' })`. `addAllViewsAsGrid`
bypasses the duplicate guard, and a duplicate panel steals each `view.canvas`.

`handleLoadVideos` scopes camera + view creation to the videos loaded **this
call** (`newVideoFiles`), not the global `state.videoFiles`, and tags each with
`vf.sessionIdx = state.activeSessionIdx`. Iterating the global list, when
loading into a pre-existing session while another session's videos exist,
skipped this view's `session.cameras` entry (so the timeline, which builds rows
from `session.cameras`, drew none) and re-created other sessions' videos here.

**Load progress.** `handleLoadSessionFolderPerCamera` drives the shared overlay
bar (`ui/loading-overlay.js`) through up to three labelled steps: **1** "Parsing
annotations: k/N cameras" (eager) or "Opening annotation files" (lazy); **2**
"Building session: k/N cameras", yielding between cameras on the pacer's clock;
**3** "Loading videos: k/N videos" (omitted when videos are deferred), painted at
N/N with "Preparing instances…" before the synchronous tail. Every camera's
`decoder.init` is started up front (each promise resolves `{decoder}` or
`{error}`, never rejects) and the per-camera loop awaits its own camera's
promise, so decoders open **in parallel** while every side effect (videoFiles,
views, decoderPool, cameras, totalFrames, fps) still happens in camera order —
the same pattern as `switchSession`. Profile with `_bench-progress-overlay.mjs
PROFILE_VIDEO=1`; `DIGEST=1` digests the loaded session for comparing loaders.
Step 2 builds each camera's Instances from the worker's columnar result via
**`addColumnarFramesToSession(session, camName, columnar, trackRemap)`** — the
same track remap / `resolveImportTrackIdx` / `score || 1.0` / occlusion /
per-node model scores (`columnar.pointScore` → `Instance.setPointScores`,
predictions only) as the nested-`frames` loop (kept for results without
`columnar`). Each Instance gets its own `Float64Array` `slice`, not a `subarray`
view, which would pin the whole camera buffer and make any structured clone of
the instance copy all of it.

**One camera, no calibration = a single-camera session.** Without a calibration
file `handleLoadSessionFolderPerCamera` gives every camera directory with a video
or a `.slp` a placeholder camera (`pose/placeholder-cameras.js`), with 3D off
until File ▸ Load Calibration. With exactly ONE such directory that is a
single-camera session: a SLEAP predictions file and its video, which need no
calibration.
- **Its camera is the directory's placeholder,** named after it, sized to the
  video by the video step, or the default 640x480 one when there is no video or
  videos are deferred. The view and the poses are on it, so single-camera Track
  All (`pose/single-camera-tracking.js`, `session.cameras.length === 1`) runs,
  and the status says it is a single-camera session.
- **The camera's own folder picked:** a picked folder with no camera
  subdirectories but a video or `.slp` at its top IS one camera, named after
  the folder. Videos under a `calibration_images/` subfolder
  (`isCalibrationVideoFile`) are never a camera's video, here or in any camera
  directory.
- **Same tracks either way:** on a real 40 fps file the folder route gives the
  same tracks as Load SLP, detection for detection.
- **Coverage:** `tests/e2e/single-camera-folder-load.mjs`, including the
  unchanged two-directory case. It fails 5 checks on the previous loader.

**Per-camera `.slp` selection.** `handleLoadSessionFolderPerCamera` loads only
**one** `.slp` per camera directory — the highest `_vN` version (first-wins on a
tie / when unversioned). A camera dir accumulates successive exports
(`<stem>_v1.slp`, `_v2.slp`, …); parsing every file would stack all versions'
instances into the same (frame, camera) slot. Skipped files are logged. The
choice lives in `loading/percam-slp-choice.js`'s `chooseCameraSlp(slps)` →
`{file, version, newer}` (re-exported here), which adds two things to a bare
max():
- **`lastModified` breaks a same-version tie** (enumeration order is arbitrary).
- **It reports when the version suffix disagrees with the disk** (`newer`, the
  most-recently-modified candidate when that is NOT the chosen file; `null`
  otherwise, including on equal mtimes). Writing fresh annotations to the
  UNVERSIONED name makes a leftover `_v1` win. The version rule is deliberately
  UNCHANGED (mtime survives neither copying nor syncing reliably, so it must not
  decide which file is authoritative); the load just says a newer file was left
  unread. Covered by `tests/test-percam-slp-choice.js` and
  `tests/e2e/percam-slp-choice-and-failure.mjs`.

**Large `.slp` → lazy loading.** The chosen `.slp` files are routed by
`shouldUseLazySlp` (`> 150 MB`): large prediction files go to a `SioLazyLoader`
instead of the eager `parseSlpH5` worker, which OOMs the tab on 100k-frame
predictions. The lazy loader is chosen when all lazy jobs are `.slp` (analysis
`.h5` folders still use `LazyFrameLoader`); a lazy-open failure surfaces an error
rather than falling back to the OOM-prone eager path. It plugs into the
`state.session.lazyLoader` seam, so rendering/scrubbing are unchanged.

**One `session.tracks` for the folder, whichever path and whichever file
opens first** — the union rule in `loading/track-union.js`. The eager loop
builds the union from every parse result BEFORE adding any camera's instances
and maps each through `remapTrackIdx`; the lazy branch takes
`lazyLoader.trackNames`, built the same way. Covered by
`tests/e2e/percam-track-union.mjs`.

**The routing decision is per FOLDER, not per file.** In a part-eager,
part-lazy folder the eager cameras populate `session.frameGroups` during load
and hydration skipped frames that already had a FrameGroup, so the lazy cameras
never showed annotations — and replacing prediction files with LUCID exports is
exactly what moves a camera across the 150 MB threshold. Unification goes toward
**LAZY, never eager** (eager OOMs on a big file; a small file on the lazy path
just hydrates on scrub). An all-small folder still loads eagerly.
`pose/triangulation.js`'s `ensureLazyFrameData` independently repairs a
partially-hydrated frame. Covered by `tests/e2e/percam-mixed-lazy-eager.mjs`.

**A `.slp` the reader chokes on is reported, not swallowed.** Parse failures
are collected per camera (`slpFailures`) and named in the status line, which
downgrades to `error`, rather than leaving an empty view under a success-styled
"Loaded N camera(s)". The worker's `f.keys()` check (see
`loading/slp-import-worker.js`) is what turns a non-HDF5 file into a rejection.

**Video load failures are surfaced, with the reason.** Every loader reports a
video the browser couldn't play via `videoLoadFailureText` (the decoder's codec
diagnosis when there is one). The per-camera path collects them in
`videoFailures` (e.g. Safari + `hev1`-tagged HEVC) and appends them to the final
status (one line per distinct diagnosis), like `slpFailures`. Lazy reopen's
attach step lists the reason per file too.

**Lazy project reopen (`handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`).** The memory-bounded "Load
Project" path for a large saved project `.slp` (routed by `handleLoadProject` in
`import-export/save-load.js` via `shouldUseLazySlp`). **It shows the progress
card, titled "Open project"** (`createOpenProgress(fileName)`, exported for the
test), one step per stage, the file name on the detail line: 1. Reading the
project file — counted in MB, from `openProjectSlp`'s `onReadBytes` (one decimal
below 100 MB); 2. Reading 3D groups and points (spinner, from the reader's
"Building labeled frames" / "Reading identities" / "Reading sessions" stage
messages, and from the last byte count, which the reader awaits, so the step
is painted before the reader goes on); 3. Restoring 3D groups — counted in frames, from
`reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy`'s `onCount` (and, for a file with
group-only identities, "Restoring identities" from
`stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups`'s `onCount`, as the same step); 4. Drawing the
first frame (spinner). A counted stage repaints at most every
`PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MS`, always shows its first count, and every call that
changes the card awaits a paint, so each step is on screen before its work.
Over a network share the open is bound by reading the file, which is why step 1
counts bytes (it used to sit on "Reading points" for minutes). Covered by
`tests/e2e/project-open-progress.mjs`. Opens the ONE interleaved
multi-camera file with `SioLazyLoader.openProjectSlp`, restores calibration +
grouping/IDs/3D from the typed `RecordingSession` via
`reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy` — 2D hydrates on scrub — and brings
up the 3D view + timeline immediately. With no decoders yet `updateTotalFrames()`
would reset to 0, so `#totalFrames` and `timeline.setTotalFrames` are written
directly from `loader.nFrames` (`timeline.setData` alone would clamp to 1). Then
`attachVideosForLazyReopen(session, loader, pickedFilesOverride)` prompts
(`forceVideoSelectionWithFolder` with `allowSkip`; a project `.slp` references
videos by path only), matches each video to a camera (parent-directory name,
camera-name-in-stem, or the referenced filename from `loader.videos`), opens
decoders in parallel, creates views/panes, rebuilds the video controller and
refines the frame counter via `updateTotalFrames`. Skippable — the session then
stays video-less and File → Load Videos still works. `pickedFilesOverride`
bypasses the prompt for tests. Covered by `tests/test-lazy-reopen.js`. Before the
first grouped frame it calls `setReprojErrorVisible(true)` when there are any
`instanceGroups`, as every other 3D-populating path does, or
`#reprojErrorSection` stays hidden (`tests/e2e/reopen-reprojection-panel-autopopulate.mjs`).
Identities come from `metadata.lucid.identities`, else (like the eager
`restoreGroupingAndUnlink`) the file's `/identity` catalog (`labels.identities`).
In the catalog case — a file from another tool, e.g. campy-live-export — the
reconstruct also reads each group's `identity_idx` (`fileIdentities`) and, unless
the file has a `frameIdentityMap`/`trackIdentityMap`, `stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups`
derives the per-detection map the Identity timeline reads. LUC3D projects take
neither step (`tests/e2e/lazy-reopen-group-identities.mjs`).

**`handleLoadMultiSession` ends with a calibration cross-check.** The tail calls
`noteSessionCalibrationDivergence(state.sessions)` LAST, once every session is
in, and queued behind the skeleton prompt (as its `onDone`) so two modals never
stack. See `ui/calibration-notice.js`.

**No calibration → placeholder cameras, so the folder can be saved.** A folder
loaded with no `calibration.toml` (and nothing embedded) gets one PLACEHOLDER
camera per view (`pose/placeholder-cameras.js`: all-zero extrinsics, f = 600,
sized from the video — the shape Load Videos always made). Per-camera loader: the
view, the video's `assignedCamera` and the folder's 2D are all keyed by the
camera directory's name (campy's camera name when it names one); the camera is
made in the video loop when the video decodes, else after it (deferred, failed or
missing video), and the cameras are then put in folder order (the order they are
written and reopened in). Single-SLP loader: `ensurePlaceholderCameras` after the video
loop (after the campy step, which compares the real calibration names), and a
recovered EMBEDDED calibration of placeholders only sets `hasCalibration` false,
so 3D stays off on reopen — as in `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`. Before, both loaders created NO camera: the `.slp`
writer writes one video per `session.cameras` entry and drops every pose whose
camera is missing, so Save wrote an empty file ("Saved (0.0 MB)"), and the
per-camera loader named views by video stem — `cam1/video.mp4` and
`cam2/video.mp4` were both `video`, and the folder's own poses (`cam1`, `cam2`)
reached no view. A placeholder is never added to a session that has a real
calibration. Test: `tests/e2e/nocalib-folder-save-reopen.mjs`.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/placeholder-cameras.js` (`makePlaceholderCamera`,
  `hasRealCalibration`, `ensurePlaceholderCameras`),
- `./calibration-pick.js` (`REBASED_CALIBRATION_NAME`, `pickCalibrationFile`),
  `./percam-slp-choice.js` (`chooseCameraSlp`), `./video-file-pick.js`,
  `./video-codec-diagnosis.js` (`videoLoadFailureText`),
  `../ui/app-state.js` (incl. `buildRememberedSkeleton`, and `isViewDocked` as the
  rebuilt video controller's `isViewShown`), `../pose/pose-data.js`,
  `../ui/browser-hints.js` (`fileSystemAccessHint`),
  `../ui/folder-confirm-tip.js` (`confirmFolderPick` — awaited before every
  folder picker; a cancel ends the load with "No folder selected"),
  `./video.js` (`OnDemandVideoDecoder`, `VideoController`, `releaseViewsFrames` —
  called wherever an outgoing session's views are stashed in `_views`: empty
  session, single-SLP folder, per-camera folder), `../import-export/file-io.js`,
  `../pose/triangulation.js` (`shouldUseLazyH5`, `shouldUseLazySlp`, `LazyFrameLoader`),
  `./sio-lazy-loader.js` (`SioLazyLoader`),
  `./track-union.js` (`unionTrackNames`, `remapTrackIdx`),
  `../import-export/save-load.js`,
  `../ui/rendering.js` (`drawAllOverlays`, `setReprojErrorVisible`),
  `../ui/info-panel.js` (`updateInfoPanel`, `promptImportSkeletonForAllSessions`),
  `../ui/calibration-notice.js` (`noteSessionCalibrationDivergence`),
  `../ui/frame-readout.js` (`refreshReadoutTotals`, wherever the loaders set
  `state.totalFrames` / `state.fps`),
  `../import-export/skeleton-json.js` (`parseSkeletonJSON`),
  `../import-export/slp-import.js`, `../ui/loading-progress-modal.js`,
  `../ui/loading-overlay.js` (`showLoadingProgress`, `createProgressPacer`,
  `yieldToPaint`), `../import-export/import-track-resolve.js`,
  `../pose/initialization.js`, `../ui/sessions-panes.js`, `../ui/ui-wiring.js`,
  `../import-export/visibility-metadata.js` (`readVisibilityMetadata`),
  `../import-export/smoothing-metadata.js` (`readSmoothingMetadata`),
  `../import-export/frame-identity-metadata.js` (`readFrameIdentityMetadata`,
  called by `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy` after the groups are rebuilt) and
  `../import-export/plane-metadata.js` (`readPlaneMetadata`, `resetPlaneState`,
  `planeMetadataConflict`), `../import-export/slp-provenance.js`
  (`fileSessionProvenance`, `fileSessionNames` — the single-SLP folder and lazy
  project loaders keep the file's provenance on the session).

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`,
`ui/sessions-panes.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**User-facing features.** File menu Load Calibration / Load Videos /
Load Session Folder / Load Multi-Session (plus the lazy Load Project path for
large project `.slp`s, incl. its attach-videos prompt), all video-to-camera
auto-matching, session-folder mode chooser. `handleLoadSessionFolder` calls
`ensureNo3dImportBlockingLoad()` first, so loading a session over a
skeleton-only 3D-points import prompts before discarding it.

---
