### loading/sio-lazy-loader.js

**Purpose.** Main-thread lazy frame loader for large prediction `.slp` files,
backed by sleap-io.js's streaming lazy reader (`readSlpStreaming({ lazy: true })`).
Drop-in for `LazyFrameLoader`'s interface (the `state.session.lazyLoader` seam),
but holds one lazy sleap-io.js `Labels` per camera on the main thread (the
reader's own worker does the HDF5 I/O) rather than a per-camera worker. Frames
are materialized on demand via `labels.frameAt(row)`, so `getFrameSync` is
synchronous.

**Key export.** class `SioLazyLoader`:
- `open(camName, file, onProgress)` — reads metadata + builds a
  videoFrameIdx→store-row map; the skeleton is the first camera BY NAME's and
  `trackNames` is the union over every opened camera (see "Track indices"), so
  nothing depends on which parallel open resolves first; returns THIS camera's
  own track names.
- `openProjectSlp(file, onProgress, onReadBytes)` — `onReadBytes(done, total)`
  (optional, awaited) counts the bytes read of the four big tables, which the
  vendored reader then reads in row slices (LUCID local patch, open progress);
  without it the read is unchanged. Lazy reopen of a SINGLE multi-camera
  project `.slp` (Load Project for large projects): one interleaved store shared
  by every camera, split into the same per-camera maps. Sets `_sharedStore =
  true` so the streaming re-save appends the store ONCE, retains `videoIdByCam`
  (camName → NATIVE store video id) for the re-save's video-id remap, computes
  each camera's `trackOccupancy` at load, and returns `{labels, typedSession,
  cameraNames, nFrames}` for `reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy`.
- `getFrame` / `getFrameSync` — typed instances → `{trackIdx (-1 = none, as in
  the store; hydration maps it to `null`), score, type, points, occluded}`,
  LRU-cached. `prefetch`, `close` (also clears `videoIdByCam`).
- Fields `nFrames`, `skeleton`, `trackNames`, `videos`, `trackOccupancy`,
  `videoIdByCam` (`null` on the per-camera path), `isSync = true` (so
  `batchLoadLazyFrames` takes its worker-free path), and `sourceFiles` (camName →
  the `File` it was opened from — a cheap lazy handle, kept so the multi-session
  streaming save's pass 2 can reopen the same cameras,
  `reopenSessionLazyLoader` in `import-export/save-load.js`).

**Track indices: one list, every store re-indexed into it (`_unifyTracks`).**
`session.tracks` for a per-camera folder is the union of the cameras' own track
names (`loading/track-union.js`). `open()` records the camera's OWN names in
`_trackSourceByCam` (`{names, toSession}`) and calls `_unifyTracks(newCam)`,
which re-derives the union FROM THE OWN NAMES (never from a previous union —
that would make it order-dependent), rewrites each camera's
`instancesData.track` when its indices moved (out-of-list values become `-1`),
rebuilds `labels.tracks` IN PLACE to the union (shared with
`_lazyDataStore.tracks`), refreshes occupancy and clears invalidated frame
caches. Afterwards every column value is a `trackNames` index in every camera —
what `adaptTypedInstance`, `forEachInstanceRow`, `_computeSparseOccupancy`,
`remapTracksFromIdentity`, Custom Delete and the streaming writer all assume —
and the writer's name-signature dedup writes the tracks ONCE. Because
`reopenSessionLazyLoader` re-derives the same columns through `open()`,
`_unifyTracks` does NOT set `_storeEditedInMemory`, while
`remapTracksFromIdentity` and `deleteInstanceRows` do (the multi-session save
then keeps the live columns for pass 2; see `import-export/save-load.js`).
`remapTracksFromIdentity` resets each camera's own names to the propagated list,
so a later `_unifyTracks` starts from that. Covered by
`tests/e2e/percam-track-union.mjs`.

`trackOccupancy` is populated per camera by `_computeSparseOccupancy(labels,
nFrames, rowMap?)` — one O(nInstances) pass over the columnar store emitting
**sparse** per-track run-segments `{ sparse:true, nTracks, nFrames,
segments:Map<trackIdx,[{start,end}]>, counts:Map<trackIdx,frameCount> }`, never
a dense nFrames×nTracks grid. The optional `rowMap` (videoFrameIdx → store row)
restricts the scan to one camera's rows and is **required** whenever the store
is SHARED across cameras (`openProjectSlp`, or `remapTracksFromIdentity`'s
rebuild), else another camera's rows mix in. Relies on the SLP on-disk frame
ordering (same invariant `appendStore` assumes); zero frame materialization.
With a `rowMap` it verifies frame order in one O(n) pass and only `.sort()`s
when a row is genuinely out of order (`tests/e2e/occupancy-sort-skip-optimization.mjs`).
`session.trackOccupancy` picks it up (`session-loader.js`); the timeline reads
the `sparse` flag (`_buildTrackSegments`, `ui/timeline.js`). Regression:
`tests/test-lazy-reopen.js`'s "propagateIdentitiesToTracks rebuilds the lazy
loader's trackOccupancy" test.

**`deleteInstanceRows(shouldDeleteFn, opts)` — the durable-delete primitive**
(Custom Instance Delete and every interactive delete, via
`deleteTargetsFromStore` in `ui/custom-delete-ops.js`). Permanently removes
instance rows from the columnar store so a delete survives eviction,
re-hydration, save and reload. Returns `{deleted, errorRows, firstError,
byCamera}` (per-row `try/catch`, like `remapTracksFromIdentity`). A
resident-only delete fails twice: `finalizeLazyFrameGroup` puts any store row
with no matching `_rawInstIndex` member into the UNLINKED pool (scrub away and
back resurrects it), and `appendStore` copies the columns verbatim while the
save's user-correction overlay skips camera-frames with no resident *user*
instance. Mutating the store fixes both.
- **One keypress must not cost a bulk pass.** `opts.only` (camName → frame
  indices, a Map's keys or a Set) limits the predicate to those camera-frames;
  compaction runs **in place from the first deleted row** (one `copyWithin` per
  surviving run up to `DELETE_RUN_COPY_MAX` (4096) deleted rows, a plain loop
  beyond); a typed column is re-exposed as a shorter `subarray`, or `slice`d to a
  right-sized copy when more than half the rows went; and `trackOccupancy` is
  rebuilt only for a camera that LOST a row. In place is safe because every
  reader indexes `store.instancesData.<col>` element-wise and fresh; an array
  under two column names is compacted once. Pinned by
  `tests/test-custom-delete-store.js`'s "one keypress, not one bulk pass" suite.
- Compacts every `instancesData` column of length `nInst` (iterates
  `Object.keys`, so a schema addition is carried through). **Leaves
  `pointsData`/`predPointsData` alone on purpose** — `appendStore` walks points
  per surviving instance via `point_id_start/end`, so orphaned point rows are
  never written.
- **Keeps frame rows** (`frameRowByCam`, `refFor`, `releaseFrame` and
  `_computeSparseOccupancy` depend on row indexing). A frame collapsed to
  `start === end` is written by `appendStore` as an empty `LabeledFrame`.
- Renumbers via a prefix sum (`survBefore`), so it does **not** assume frame rows
  are sorted by `instance_id_start`. Remaps `from_predicted` through the same
  table, degrading a deleted target to `-1` (as `appendStore`'s `outIdxOf` does).
- Groups cameras by their `labels` so a **shared store** is compacted EXACTLY
  ONCE — the guard covers the whole mutation, since compaction is global to a
  store. Then clears both cache layers (`this.cache` +
  `labels._lazyFrameList.clearCache()`).
- **Caller contract:** store-only. The caller must also renumber `_rawInstIndex`
  on surviving instances in each touched (camera, frame) — else `refFor` writes
  grouping refs at the wrong instances and hydration loads the wrong 2D — and
  mirror the removal into `frameGroups`/`instanceGroups` under one shared `seen`
  Set.
- Unit tests: `tests/test-custom-delete-store.js`.

**Memory bounding.** `open()` sets each camera's `labels.frameCacheLimit`
(default 512) so sleap-io.js FIFO-bounds its typed-frame cache.
`releaseFrame(frameIdx)` / `releaseWindow(start, end)` drop a frame (or half-open
range) from BOTH the loader's adapted-dict LRU AND each camera's lazy `Labels`
(public `labels.releaseFrame(row)`), used by the windowed triangulate-all /
streaming-export sweeps. `store.materializeFrame` rebuilds a dropped frame on
next access, so release is safe.

**Project-wide identity/track propagation** (reaches every frame, not only
resident ones):
- `forEachInstanceRow(visitFn, opts)` — read-only sweep over every
  `(camName, frameIdx, trackIdx)` instance triple straight from each camera's
  columnar store, zero materialization. Used by
  `Session.propagateTracksToIdentities`. Optional `{camera, start, end}` narrows
  it to one camera and/or `[start, end)`, visited in ascending frame order (the
  status bar's whole-project counters, `ui/frame-counters.js`).
- `remapTracksFromIdentity(newTrackNames, remapFn)` — the write-side companion,
  used by `Session.propagateIdentitiesToTracks`: rebuilds each `labels.tracks` in
  place (a shared store only once), then writes `remapFn(camName, frameIdx,
  oldTrackIdx, offsetInFrame)` into `instancesData.track` — the array
  `appendStore` and `materializeFrame` read by reference, so the propagated track
  survives eviction and export with no writer plumbing. `offsetInFrame` (=
  members' `_rawInstIndex`) lets a caller decide per ROW rather than per track
  (two rows can share a raw trackIdx and need different answers); 3-argument
  callbacks (`swapTracksInStore` in `ui/identity-assignment.js`) are unaffected.
  It rebuilds this camera's `trackOccupancy` — `session.trackOccupancy` is the
  SAME Map (aliased in `session-loader.js`) and the timeline trusts it for every
  unmaterialized frame — and invalidates both cache layers via
  `_lazyFrameList.clearCache()` (per-row `releaseFrame` fallback if the bundle
  lacks it). Both methods are duck-typed from `Session`, so the worker-backed
  `LazyFrameLoader` (analysis `.h5`) is unaffected.
  **Error handling.** `frameIdentityMap`/`instanceGroups`/resident tracks are
  already fixed up before this runs, so an abort mid-remap would leave "GUI looks
  right, export broken past some point". Each row is try/caught (a bad row keeps
  its OLD track), a per-camera console summary is logged, and it returns
  `{changed, errorRows, firstError}`; `Session.propagateIdentitiesToTracks` folds
  `errorRows` into its own return and `ui/ui-wiring.js` reports a nonzero
  `lazyErrorRows` as an error status.

**`describeStoreFrame(camName, frameIdx)`** — what re-hydrating one
camera-frame WOULD build, read straight from the columns: `{ count, trackIdx:
Int32Array, predicted: Uint8Array }` (entry `k` = the row an `_rawInstIndex` of
`k` names; trackless and a track id with no `Track` behind it are -1; an absent
`instance_type` reads as 0, a user row, as `materializeFrame` defaults it),
`count: 0` for a frame with no row, null for a camera this loader does not back.
The playback eviction (`pose/lazy-residency.js`) proves a resident frame
rebuildable against it; `tests/e2e/lazy-playback-eviction.mjs` pins it to the
real materializer.

**`predictedPointScore(camName, frameIdx, offsetInFrame, nodeName)`** — the
model's score for one node of the store's prediction at that in-frame offset,
from `pred_points.score` via `point_id_start`: the lazy half of
`Session.predictedNodeScore`. Nothing is materialized or kept on the instance.
The node is looked up BY NAME in the store's skeleton, because a skeleton edit
re-indexes resident instances but not the store; an added or renamed node has
no score. NaN for a user row, an offset outside the frame's rows, a missing
frame/camera, or a NaN score. Pinned by `tests/test-node-scores.mjs` and
`tests/e2e/node-score-tooltip.mjs`.

**Imports.** `./track-union.js` (`unionTrackNames`);
`window.SleapIO.readSlpStreaming` / `window.SleapIO.Track` (via the index.html
bridge) and the local vendored `lib/h5wasm/h5wasm.iife.js` (passed as
`h5wasmUrl`).

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js`
(`handleLoadSessionFolderPerCamera` routing, `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`) and
`import-export/save-load.js` (`reopenSessionLazyLoader`). `describeStoreFrame` is
called duck-typed from `pose/lazy-residency.js`, `predictedPointScore` from
`Session.predictedNodeScore` (`pose/pose-data.js`).

**User-facing features.** Lets a session folder of large multi-camera prediction
`.slp` files — and a large saved project `.slp` (Load Project) — load and render
without OOMing the tab. Lazy project reopen is covered by
`tests/test-lazy-reopen.js`.

---

### loading/slp-import-worker.js

**Purpose.** Web Worker that runs h5wasm in a separate thread to parse
and lazily index SLP HDF5 files. Mounts File objects via WORKERFS for
zero-copy access. Two modes: full eager parse, or lazy
open-and-stream-frames.

**Message protocol.**
- IN: `{type: 'parse', file, slowCompound?, columnar?}` — full eager parse.
  `slowCompound: true` forces h5wasm's `Dataset.value` for the compound tables
  (the equivalence test's reference path; also a kill switch). `columnar: true`
  returns the poses as flat TRANSFERRED typed arrays in `data.columnar` (and an
  empty `data.frames`) — see below.
- IN: `{type: 'open', file}` — lazy open, return metadata only.
- IN: `{type: 'getFrame', frameIdx, requestId}` — read one frame lazily.
- IN: `{type: 'getFrames', startIdx, endIdx, requestId}` — read range.
- IN: `{type: 'close'}` — close lazy file.
- OUT: `{type: 'progress', message}`, `{type: 'result', data: {...}}`,
  `{type: 'metadata', data: {...}}`, `{type: 'frameData', ...}`,
  `{type: 'framesData', ...}`, `{type: 'error', message}`.

**An unopenable file must reject, not resolve empty.** `new h5wasm.File(path,
'r')` does **not** throw on non-HDF5 bytes, and every `f.get()` in `parseSlp` is
try/caught to null (right for optional datasets), so a non-SLP file would post
an ordinary `result` with 0 frames. `parseSlp` therefore checks `f.keys()` right
after the open and throws `Not a readable HDF5/SLP file (no root datasets)`.
Covered by `tests/e2e/percam-slp-choice-and-failure.mjs`.

**Fast compound reads (`readCompoundColumnsFast`).** SLEAP-written `.slp` files
store `frames` / `instances` / `points` / `pred_points` as HDF5 **compound**
datasets, and h5wasm's `Dataset.value` boxes every cell (an 8-camera folder load
took 72 s; ~5 s with this). `readColumnar`/`readPoints` first try one
`get_dataset_data` + a DataView decode per member into a `Float64Array` — a port
of sleap-io.js's `readCompoundColumnsWorker` (keep the decode rules in step).
Columns are matched BY NAME (the `.value` path maps by position); anything that
is not a plain-numeric 1-D compound containing every requested field returns
null and falls back to `.value`. LUCID's own files (2-D matrix + `field_names`)
never take this path. `tests/e2e/slp-import-fast-compound.mjs` requires both
paths to give `Object.is`-equal results, on
`tests/fixtures/slp-compound/sleap-compound-small.slp` (a Python sleap-io file,
regenerable with `make_fixture.py`) and with `DATASET=` on real files.

**Columnar result (`buildColumnarFrames`).** The default `data.frames` (boxed
`[x, y]` arrays + an object per instance) is expensive to structured-clone. With
`columnar: true` the worker instead returns `data.columnar` = `{ numNodes,
nFrames, nInstances, frameIdx, videoIdx, instOffsets (nFrames+1), trackIdx,
score, type (1 = predicted), xy (NaN = missing), occluded, pointScore }` — the same
frames/instances and values, as typed arrays TRANSFERRED in the `postMessage`.
Only `parseSlpH5(file, null, { columnar: true })` from
`handleLoadSessionFolderPerCamera` asks for it. The equivalence test also
requires the columnar result to expand back to exactly the nested `frames`.

**A prediction's per-node scores (`pred_points.score`)** ride along both ways:
each nested instance carries `pointScores` (a `Float64Array(numNodes)`, NaN where
a node has none; null for a user instance or a prediction read from `points`),
and the columnar result a flat `pointScore` (`[i*numNodes + k]`, NaN for user
instances). The importers keep them with `Instance.setPointScores`. **Not for
analysis `.h5` files**, deliberately: `parseAnalysisH5` drops `point_scores`; the
NOTE there says what showing them would take.

**A File that is still downloading can read back with zeros** (Google Drive for
desktop on macOS). WORKERFS's read is guarded in the vendored h5wasm itself
(`lucidColdReadGuard`, the `LUCID local patch (cold-read)` in both builds — see
`lib/h5wasm/PROVENANCE.txt` and CLAUDE.md), which covers this worker's eager
parse and lazy analysis `.h5`, `frame-worker.js` and sleap-io.js's streaming
worker alike. A recovery logs `[h5wasm WORKERFS] bytes … were read again`.
`tests/test-workerfs-cold-read.mjs` runs this worker in Node over a File with
injected holes.

**Imports from project modules.** None (only the vendored
`../lib/h5wasm/hdf5_hl.js`).

**Imported by.** Spawned via
`new Worker(new URL('loading/slp-import-worker.js?v=' + Date.now(), document.baseURI), {type: 'module'})`
from `import-export/file-io.js` (eager parse) and `pose/triangulation.js`
(lazy reads, `LazyFrameLoader`). The `document.baseURI` resolution makes the
URL work on sub-path deployments (GitHub Pages `/luc3d/`, `/luc3d/pr/N/`).

**User-facing features.** SLP import progress without freezing the UI;
lazy frame loading for very large SLP files.

---

### loading/slp-package-reader.js

**Purpose.** HDF5 helper class for reading frame-extracted `.pkg.slp`
files. Knows how to enumerate `videoN` groups, read PNG/JPG byte
strings, and translate display frames ↔ embedded indices. Designed to
run inside a worker context with h5wasm available.

**Key exports.**
- `SLPPackageReader` — class. Methods: `open(url)` (range-request
  streaming), `openFile(h5File)`, `close`, `getVideos`,
  `getVideoInfo(videoKey)`, `getFrame(videoKey, embeddedIdx)`,
  `findEmbeddedIndex(videoKey, displayFrame)`,
  `findClosestFrame(videoKey, displayFrame)`,
  `hasFrame(videoKey, displayFrame)`, `getFrameRange(videoKey)`.

**Imports from project modules.** None (uses h5wasm passed in via
options bag).

**Imported by.** `loading/frame-worker.js`.

**User-facing features.** Backs frame extraction for `.pkg.slp` files
loaded over the network or from disk.

---

### loading/cold-read-guard.js

**Purpose.** Blob reads that survive a file still downloading from a
cloud-synced folder. In a Google Drive for desktop folder on macOS (File
Provider), a read of a part of a file that is not downloaded yet can FAIL
(`arrayBuffer()` rejects with NotReadableError, a Blob stream errors with
`TypeError: network error`) or come back with a run of ZEROS and every other
byte right — the failure lib/h5wasm's `LUCID local patch (cold-read)` guards
for every h5wasm reader. Drive fetches a video only in the ranges that are read,
so the load AND every seek into an unvisited part of a video can hit it.
Measured numbers are in CLAUDE.md; unguarded, 2 of 48 cold cameras lost their
frame-accurate decoder, and through the guard every failure was rescued and
every zeroed run repaired.

**The rule.** `readBlobRange(blob, start, end)` retries a read that throws,
after `COLD_READ_RETRY_MS` (200 ms, 1 s, 3 s), then rethrows the last error;
and re-reads every all-zero part of a read (per 4 KiB block of the FILE,
clipped to the read, neighbours merged — `zeroRuns`) together with the 4 KiB
block on each side of it, copying re-read bytes in only where the first read
gave a zero byte, so the guard cannot make a read worse. The neighbours are
there because a run of zeros can start or end part-way into a block, which is
then not all zero (every wrong block on cold `.slp` files through h5wasm's first
version of the rule, 93 of 93). The repaired range is checked again the same
way, at most three re-reads deep — h5wasm's `lucidColdReadGuard` rule, plus the
retry. Repairs and rescues log a `[cold-read]`
console warning and are counted in `coldReadStats`.

**Key exports.**
- `readBlobRange(blob, start, end)` → `Promise<Uint8Array>`.
- `readBlobArrayBuffer(blob)` — the whole blob, guarded; a stand-in for
  `blob.arrayBuffer()`.
- `guardedRangeSource(blob, opts?)` → `{ size, readRange(start, length) }`, the
  shape sleap-io.js's `MediaBunnyVideoBackend.fromRangeSource` takes (it drives
  mediabunny's `StreamSource` with it). Reads whole `RANGE_WINDOW_BYTES` (1 MiB)
  aligned windows rather than each ~64–192 KiB request, since a Blob read costs
  about the same however small (per-request reads were ~6x slower than
  mediabunny's `BlobSource`); keeps the most recent `RANGE_CACHE_BYTES` (4 MiB)
  and answers with copies so mediabunny's cache never pins a window. A failed
  window is dropped, so the next request reads again.
- `zeroRuns(bytes, position)`, `repairZeroRuns(blob, bytes, position, depth)`,
  `coldReadStats`, `COLD_READ_BLOCK`, `COLD_READ_RETRY_MS`,
  `RANGE_WINDOW_BYTES`, `RANGE_CACHE_BYTES`.

**Imports from project modules.** None, so a Node test can load it.

**Imported by.** `loading/video.js` (mediabunny via `guardedRangeSource`;
mp4box's scan via `readChunk`), `loading/video-codec-diagnosis.js`
(`readRange`), `import-export/slp-import.js` (`handleLoadPoints3dH5`).

**Not covered.** The `<video>` element reads its blob URL natively, where no
page code can reach (playback, and stepping only when there is no
frame-accurate backend); the small JSON / TOML `.text()` reads (a zeroed or
failed read of those fails to parse, loudly).

**Tests.** `tests/test-cold-read-guard.mjs` (the rule over a fake Blob with
injected holes and failures, including runs that start or end part-way into a
block, with the all-zero-blocks-only rule as negative control; mediabunny's index and packets of the B-frame clip
through the guard vs the unguarded read) and `tests/e2e/video-cold-read.mjs`
(the real OnDemandVideoDecoder with faults injected under it; unguarded, a
failed read leaves mp4box's scan at the `<video>` estimate and a zeroed first
page freezes the page).

### loading/video-codec-diagnosis.js

**Purpose.** Explain WHY the browser refused a video. A `<video>` load
failure only yields "error code 4" (MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED); this reads
the file's video codec from its MP4 sample description and returns a specific,
actionable message. Measured cases (`tests/e2e/_probe-capabilities.mjs`):
Safari plays HEVC-in-MP4 only when tagged `hvc1` (`hev1` → canPlayType "") — a
lossless `ffmpeg -c copy -tag:v hvc1` re-tag fixes it; Safari decodes AV1 only
with hardware AV1 (Apple M3+), while Chrome/Firefox decode it in software;
otherwise suggest another browser / converting to H.264. Cheap on multi-GB
recordings: it walks top-level box headers with random-access reads
(`Blob.slice`, or HTTP Range for URLs) and reads only `moov` (often at the
END, after the `mdat`). No diagnosis (null) when the codec can't be read or the
browser claims support — the original error then stands.

**Key exports.**
- `sniffMp4VideoCodec(source)` → `{fourcc, codecName}|null` (File/Blob/URL).
- `videoFourccFromMoov(moovBytes)` — first video sample-entry fourcc in a `moov`.
- `explainUnplayableCodec(fourcc, fileName, env?)` → `{fourcc, codecName, kind,
  message}|null`; pure (`env.canPlayType`, `env.isSafari` injectable). Kinds:
  `hevc-hev1-tag`, `av1-unsupported`, `hevc-unsupported`, `unsupported`, `encrypted`.
- `diagnoseUnplayableVideo(source, fileName?, env?)` — sniff + explain.
- `videoLoadFailureText(name, err)` — one-line reason for loaders' status
  messages: `err.codecDiagnosis.message` when present, else the error text.

**Imports from project modules.** `loading/cold-read-guard.js`
(`readBlobRange`: a cloud file still downloading).

**Imported by.** `loading/video.js` (`OnDemandVideoDecoder._awaitPlayable`),
`loading/session-loader.js` (`videoLoadFailureText`).

**Tests.** `tests/test-video-codec-diagnosis.js` (synthetic MP4s, every message
kind, decoder integration); real browsers via the `decoderErrors` step of
`tests/e2e/_probe-capabilities.html`.

### loading/video.js

**Purpose.** Video decoding and multi-view playback. Hybrid HTML5
`<video>` + WebCodecs + mp4box.js decoder for frame-accurate seeking,
plus a `VideoController` that synchronises playback across all
overlay-paired video panes. Frame-accurate frames come from the mediabunny
backend (below); without it, frame extraction runs through the HTML5
`<video>` path (`_getFrameHTML5`). mp4box is used only to recover the true
fps/frame-count, and the WebCodecs path stays off (`_mp4Initialized` never
set true) to avoid B-frame decode-order mismatches. `_getFrameHTML5`'s seek
guard uses a frame-rate-aware tolerance (half a frame period, `0.5/_fps`) so
high-fps recordings step every frame instead of freezing under a fixed
constant.

**`<video>` seeks aim at the MIDDLE of the frame (`html5SeekTime`).**
`_getFrameHTML5` sets `currentTime = (i + 0.5) / fps`, not `i / fps`. A
frame-start time sits on the boundary with frame i−1 and browsers round it
either way (Firefox showed i−1 for a third of frames; mid-interval was exact in
every Firefox seek and every H.264 seek elsewhere). Seeking to mediabunny's
exact PTS (`_frameTimes[i]`) is the frame start again and misses identically.
**`requestVideoFrameCallback` cannot verify a landing in Firefox**: its
`mediaTime` echoes the seek target, not the frame shown. `seekNativeSettled`
(play start) treats an element parked on the frame's middle as already there:
`(i+0.5)/fps − i/fps` comes out an ulp over half a frame for ~30% of frames,
and re-seeking to the frame start would land on i−1 in Firefox. **Only a
position `_getFrameHTML5` set itself skips the seek** (`_html5Moved`, set by
`playNative`, `seekNative` and a real `seekNativeSettled` seek, cleared by the
next `_getFrameHTML5` seek and by `switchSource`): after playback the picture
need not match `currentTime`, and skipping the seek on a pause re-decode drew
the NEXT frame.

**A codec WebCodecs cannot decode drops the backend up front
(`_mbCannotDecode`).** Firefox demuxes HEVC — `MediaBunnyVideoBackend`
initializes — but has no WebCodecs HEVC decoder, so every `getFrame` failed
twice before falling back. `_initMediabunny` asks mediabunny's
`track.canDecode()` once, AFTER adopting the backend's frame count and fps
(container metadata, still right), and on `false` closes the backend and sets
`_mbUnavailable = { reason: 'codec', codec, codecString }`; a failed init sets
`{ reason: 'init', message }`, and both `init` and `switchSource` reset it.
Only HEVC in Firefox answers `false`, so other browsers' paths are unchanged. A
check that cannot be asked answers null, leaving the per-frame fallback to
cover it. With no backend, the image ID-switch check's `keyframeIndices` /
`streamingReader` (`ui/image-embedder.js`) take their `getFrame` path rather
than a stream that cannot decode.

Covered by `tests/test-mediabunny-backend.js`,
`tests/test-html5-seek-tolerance.js` (the mid target, the play-start parking,
the `_html5Moved` rule) and `tests/e2e/html5-step-mid-frame.mjs` (real Chromium
made to answer "cannot decode", stepping through
`tests/fixtures/barcode-60/barcode-60.mp4` — frame number as a barcode,
regenerated by `make_fixture.sh` — and reading every frame number back; its
control decoder, seeking to the frame START, must land wrong or the test could
not tell the targets apart). The fixture is 60 fps on purpose: at 10 fps
(`bframes-test`) frame times are whole microseconds and both targets land.

**Frame-accurate mediabunny backend (default-on, issue #115).** HTML5
`<video>.currentTime` seeking is NOT frame-accurate — it can return a
frame a whole GOP behind the one requested, so the pose overlay ends up on a
stale video frame. By default `init()` builds a
`MediaBunnyVideoBackend` (from sleap-io.js, using the vendored
`lib/mediabunny/`) via `_initMediabunny(source)` and adopts its
authoritative frame count / fps; `getFrame()` then decodes exact frames
through mediabunny, transparently falling back to the HTML5 seek on any
init/decode failure. `_mediabunnyEnabled()` is ON unless
`LUCID_VIDEO_BACKEND` (from `window` or `localStorage`) is `'html5'` /
`'legacy'` — default-on (rather than opt-in) because `localStorage` is
per-origin, so an opt-in flag silently disables the fix on any origin
where it wasn't set (a PR preview vs localhost). It can't be validated
headless (headless *software* decode is itself frame-inaccurate). The bug is
display-only; pose data imports and exports correctly regardless.

**The vendored backend's frame index must be in PRESENTATION order.**
`initialize()` (`lib/sleap-io/chunk-X76PRJK6.js`) built `_frameTimes` from
`EncodedPacketSink.packets()`, which yields DECODE order, so on any B-frame
video `decodeSingleFrame(i)` returned the wrong frame. A local patch sorts
`_frameTimes` ascending — see the `(#115, decode-order)` entry in CLAUDE.md;
reported upstream as sleap-io.js#262. Covered by
`tests/e2e/mediabunny-bframe-decode-order.mjs` (fixture video + PNGs
regenerated together by `tests/fixtures/bframes-test/make_fixture.sh`).

**The frame index is built metadata-only (`luc3d frame-index` patch).** The
same walk passes `{ metadataOnly: true }` so it does not read every packet's
payload (the whole video) just to collect timestamps — ~120 → ~28 ms per
camera. Identical packets/order/timestamps, pinned by
`tests/e2e/mediabunny-frame-index-metadata-only.mjs`. See the
`(luc3d frame-index)` entry in CLAUDE.md.

**`switchSource()` must refresh `_mbBackend` too.**
`switchSource(source)` — used by the pooled-decoder session-switch/reopen path
(`ui/sessions-panes.js`'s `switchSession()`, which swaps the source rather than
creating a new `<video>` element, to dodge Chrome browser-process crashes from
element churn) — closes the old `_mbBackend` and re-runs
`_initMediabunny(source)`, mirroring `init()`. Otherwise every frame-accurate
`getFrame()` after a switch silently decodes from the PREVIOUS video. Covered
by `tests/e2e/switchsource-mediabunny-refresh.mjs` (checks decoded pixels, since
the fixture videos share a frame count).

**The `<video>` `error` listener reads the ELEMENT it was attached to, not
`this._videoEl`.** Both `init()` and `switchSource()` register a `once: true`
`error` listener that rejects their metadata promise. It OUTLIVES a successful
load, and `close()` ends with `src = ""` + `load()`, which fires one last
`error` asynchronously after `close()` has nulled `this._videoEl` — so reading
`self._videoEl.error` threw on every closed decoder. Both sites capture
`var el = this._videoEl` at registration and read `el.error`.

**`getFrame()` checks `_mbBackend` BEFORE the shared `this.cache`.** That cache
is also written by `_getFrameHTML5` (`addToCache`), so checking it first let a
one-off HTML5 fallback permanently shadow mediabunny for that frame index with
a frame-inaccurate bitmap. mediabunny keeps its own cache; `this.cache` holds
only HTML5/WebCodecs fallback results. Covered in
`tests/test-mediabunny-backend.js`.

**`getFrame()` serializes calls into `_mbBackend`.** The backend's single-frame
decode has no internal queue (only `decodeRange` does), so overlapping
`getFrame()` calls from rapid stepping could return the wrong frame.
`_mbSeekLock` around calls into `_mbBackend.getFrame` matches
`_getFrameHTML5`'s `_html5SeekLock`. Covered (stubbed backend) in
`tests/test-mediabunny-backend.js`.

**Paused stepping keeps a decode stream open (`_mbGetFrame`).**
`MediaBunnyVideoBackend.getFrame` opens a fresh decoder per frame and decodes
from the frame's keyframe, so every paused step decoded up to a GOP per camera.
`getFrame` goes through `_mbGetFrame`, which keeps one mediabunny
`sink.samples(t)` stream per decoder (`_stepCursor`) and serves any request at
or after its position that needs no newer keyframe (`_sameKeyframeRun`) by
advancing it; a step back or a jump past the next keyframe reopens it at the
target. Results go into the backend's frame cache. Released after `STEP_CURSOR_IDLE_MS` (3 s) idle, by
`releaseStepCursor()` (called for every view by
`VideoController.startPlayback`), `switchSource` and `close`. Any failure falls
back to the backend's own `getFrame`; `window.LUCID_STEP_CURSOR = 0` turns it
off. A step forward went ~148 → 1 ms (8 cameras, keyframe every 250); stepped
frames are pixel-identical to the frame-accurate decode. Cost while paused:
each open stream holds a few read-ahead frames and a decoder until it idles out.
**Stepping BACK decodes in chunks (`_decodeBackChunk`).** A frame can only be
decoded forward from its keyframe, so when a request is 1–3 frames before the
previous one, `_mbGetFrame` decodes that run once and caches up to
`STEP_BACK_CHUNK` (24) frames ending at the target, never earlier than its
keyframe, so the next steps back are cache hits. Capped at 40% of the frame
cache, so the chunk on screen and the one being prefetched both fit;
`window.LUCID_STEP_BACK_CHUNK = 0` turns it off.
**Background prefetch + landing warm-up.** While stepping back, `_prefetchBack`
decodes the chunk before the cached run in the background (its own decoder, not
under `_mbSeekLock`; one at a time; a request for a frame it is decoding waits for
it), and `_scheduleBackWarm` does the same `STEP_BACK_WARM_MS` (250 ms) after the
user LANDS on a frame by a jump and stays — counted from when the frame is
shown, so back-to-back jumps never start one. Anything but a step back cancels
it (`job.cancelled`, checked per decoded frame; `releaseStepCursor` too). Chunk
frames are cached by `_cacheNear`, which evicts the frame FARTHEST from the
user, not the least recently used: stepping back, the not-yet-reached
prefetched frames ARE the least recently used, and LRU evicted exactly them.
`window.LUCID_STEP_BACK_PREFETCH = 0` turns prefetch and warm-up off. Chunking
fills the cache sooner but does not raise its ceiling. Guarded by
`tests/e2e/step-cursor.mjs`.

**Callers must coalesce rapid single-frame steps via `scrubToFrame`, never
call `seekToFrame` directly for repeatable user input.** With backend calls
serialized (`_mbSeekLock`), uncoalesced rapid presses queue FULLY IN ORDER, so
every intermediate frame decodes and paints before the display catches up.
`scrubToFrame()` coalesces to only the LATEST requested target
(`_scrubTarget`/`_isSeeking`); the arrow keys and Home/End in
`ui/ui-wiring.js`, `seekToLabeledFrame` (alt+arrow), the seekbar drag and
`navigateToFrame`'s real-video branch (`pose/initialization.js`, the transport
Next/Prev/First/Last buttons) all go through it. Covered by
`tests/test-video-controller.js` and
`tests/e2e/arrow-key-coalesced-stepping.mjs`.

**`resumeAfterScrub(prepare)` — play on from where a scrub lands.** A scrub
stops playback, so the seekbar (on release of a press that began while
playing) asks for it back. It waits for the scrub queue to drain
(`_scrubSettled`, resolved by `_processScrub` once no target is in flight or
queued), then for the optional `prepare(frame)` promise (the app hydrates a
lazy project's frames ahead, as Play does), then `startPlayback()` at
`state.currentFrame`. Starting before the scrub drains would let a queued seek
paint over the playing video. It stays pending (`resumePending`) until playback
starts, and any later `scrubToFrame` or `stopPlayback` cancels it — so an arrow
step in that window leaves the video paused, while a second seekbar click reads
`resumePending` as "still playing". Covered by
`tests/e2e/seekbar-click-keeps-playback.mjs`.

**Playback overlay/video sync.** During playback the frame-info / timeline
updates are throttled and cut down (status-bar counters skipped while playing,
`ui/info-panel.js`; timeline playhead-only redraw, `ui/timeline.js`) so the
overlay does not drift ahead of the video, and per-frame `[3D]` logging is
gated behind `window.LUCID_3D_DEBUG`. Native playback is the default;
`VideoController.stopPlayback` fires a final unthrottled overlay/seekbar update
so the info panel and timeline settle to the exact stop frame. The 3D viewport
is **not throttled** (10 Hz made it jump 6–7 frames; `updateSkeleton` is cheap
enough per frame).
**The native loop is per-refresh** (`_playbackLoopMode() === 'refresh'`,
default): on every `requestAnimationFrame` it captures each view's current
frame (`decoder.captureCurrentFrame()` → `new VideoFrame(<video>)` + the index
from that frame's own timestamp), `drawImage`s exactly that frame, and calls
`drawOverlays(mainIdx, viewFrames)` so each view is overlaid at its own frame;
a refresh where no view's frame changes draws nothing, captures are always
`close()`d (held ones on `stopPlayback` via `_refreshCleanup`), and playback
stops when the primary `<video>` ends.
- **Browsers whose `VideoFrame(<video>)` timestamps don't track the picture**
  are detected per decoder by `judgeVideoFrameTimestamps` (the timestamp must
  move, agree with the clock within max(3 frames, 50 ms) — 3 frames alone
  misjudged Chrome at 150 fps on 60 Hz — and not BE the clock; cached as
  `decoder._vfTimestamps = 'ok'|'bad'`). **WebKit never captures at all**
  (`videoFrameCaptureUnsafe()`): Safari's capture timestamp rarely names the
  captured picture yet can pass judging, and captures cost it presented frames.
  While a view is still being judged it is not drawn.
- A `'bad'` view stops capturing, is painted with `drawCurrentFrame`, and is
  indexed from a per-view requestVideoFrameCallback. **Safari** (one callback
  per shown picture) is painted INSIDE the callback at its `mediaTime`, but only
  once the reported frame first changes (the first callback after the pre-play
  seek carries the pre-seek frame). **Firefox** (coalesced callbacks:
  `rvfcCallbacksCoalesced()`) is painted on the refresh and indexed by a
  **`CoalescedFrameClock`** evaluated at the instant of the draw; each callback
  bounds the painted frame's due time to one refresh, so the loop skips an
  uncertain refresh when the next would be certain (≤ 2 in a row, only at
  ≤ ~½ frame per refresh) and otherwise draws the midpoint guess. A Firefox
  estimate never steps backwards. With no rVFC, the clock. (Deferring Firefox's
  draw to a timer was tried and dropped: its timers slip under load.)
- **Until a fallback view's first usable callback its index is unknown and it
  is not drawn**: its canvas still shows the paused `startFrame`, which is
  what it is overlaid at (the clock is no stand-in). rVFC is registered only for fallback views
  (registering it on every view altered Chrome's presentation).
  `self._refreshFallback` exposes the fallback state (diagnostics only).
- WHICH frame each view shows is chosen by the pure **`PlaybackSchedule`** and
  **`pickScheduledFrame(target, shown, pending, cap)`** (per view: the frame on
  screen, ONE capture held from an earlier refresh, or this refresh's capture —
  hold while the target hasn't passed the shown frame; else the newest frame
  not past the target, else the oldest one past it; never run ahead). The
  schedule only acts when a video frame spans MORE than one refresh
  (frames/refresh < 0.9, e.g. 60 fps on 120 Hz): a frame clock locked to the
  rAF timestamps, run one unit BEHIND the captures, drift-corrected only for
  genuine drift, re-anchored on stall/seek/rate change. Otherwise it returns
  the primary's capture unchanged. **A SECONDARY view's target is clamped to the
  frame the primary reached this refresh (`min(target, shown[0])`)**: without
  it the free-running clock reaches a frame only some views have captured, two
  cameras sit one frame apart, and the overlay (drawn at the primary's index)
  disagrees with the secondary's canvas. That only happens on a >60 Hz display,
  so headless runs cannot see it.
- The previous loop — everything redrawn from the PRIMARY view's
  `requestVideoFrameCallback` `mediaTime` — is opt-in via
  `window.LUCID_PLAYBACK_LOOP='rvfc'`. It was replaced because Chrome presented
  that one off-page primary `<video>` at a varying rate, capping every canvas
  at it while other cameras' overlays sat frames off their video.
Covered by `tests/test-playback-frame-sync.js` (the cadence against modelled
captures, `CoalescedFrameClock` on simulated Firefox callbacks, and the
"default refresh loop" block).

*Buffered mediabunny playback (`_startBufferedPlayback`) is OPT-IN* via
`window.LUCID_PLAYBACK_BACKEND='buffered'`/`'mediabunny'`
(`_bufferedPlaybackEnabled()`). It is VIDEO-LED and frame-accurate — a
producer (`pump(view)`) decodes CHUNK-sized ranges ahead of the playhead
into each backend's LRU cache (serialized per backend via `view._mbBusy`;
`cacheSize` bumped to `W+CHUNK+margin` and restored on stop), and a
wall-clock rAF loop advances `drawn` only to the newest frame `<= target`
decoded in EVERY view, then `paint(f)` draws each view's cached bitmap AND
the overlay for that SAME `f` synchronously, so the overlay can never lead
the video. But WebCodecs can't sustain real-time multi-view HEVC decode, so it
plays in choppy spurts — hence opt-in. Tunables: `LUCID_PLAYBACK_BUFFER`
(frames ahead, default `max(24, fps)`), `LUCID_PLAYBACK_CHUNK` (default 12),
`LUCID_PLAYBACK_DEBUG` (per-second fps / draw-ms / overlay-ms / underrun
readout). The sync invariant (overlay never leads) is unit-tested with a mock
backend (`tests/test-playback-buffered.js`).

**Zoom/pan resize anchoring.** The pan offset (`view.zoom.offsetX/offsetY`) is
screen-space px relative to the wrapper's base display size
(`zoom.baseW/baseH`, recorded by `applyZoom`); on a cell resize `reapplyZoom`
rescales it by `newBase/oldBase` before re-clamping, so the same region stays
centered. A pane
rebuild (grid ↔ solo) is the same case on a NEW canvas: the view keeps its
`zoom` (with the old base) and `refreshPaneInteractions` calls `reapplyZoom`
once the new box is fitted (see `ui/sessions-panes.js`, including the one
exception, View ▸ Native Resolution).

**Wheel zoom stays under the cursor — through the WHOLE transform.**
`zoomVideo(view, factor, cssX, cssY)` anchors through `_zoomAbout`, which
inverts `applyZoom`'s full transform: a canvas-box point u is drawn at
`flex + off + c + R·(u∘s − c)` (the wrapper's flex-centring offset in its cell,
then its rotation R about its own centre c). Dropping the flex term makes a
letterboxed axis slide out from under the cursor. An axis whose picture still
FITS its pane is re-centred by `_constrainOffsets` on every step regardless.
Three helpers every zoom shares: `_zoomFrame(view)` (c, the flex offset f and
R), `_boxPointAt(view, qx, qy, frame)` (screen → box, per-axis scale included)
and `_panToDraw(view, ux, uy, sx, sy, qx, qy, frame)` (the pan that draws box
point u at screen point q). `_zoomAbout` is the last two in a row.

**A box zoom shows the box — through the same transform.**
`zoomToRect(view, x1, y1, x2, y2, container)` maps the dragged rectangle's
corners and centre through `_boxPointAt`, sizes the new uniform scale from the
corners' extent in SCREEN axes (`R·u`, so any rotation and a per-axis 1:1 start
state work), then `_panToDraw`s the centre to the container centre (a plain
`(x − off) / s` drops the flex term and the rotation). Covered by
`tests/e2e/box-zoom-letterbox.mjs`.

**Native resolution (1:1).** View ▸ Native Resolution / `Shift+0`
(`setNativeResolution`, `toggleNativeResolution`) shows a view at ONE VIDEO
PIXEL PER DEVICE PIXEL. It is a zoom scale, not a second display path: the
backing canvas is already the video's native size, only the wrapper's CSS
transform scales it. Two things are needed and each alone breaks it:
- **A scale PER AXIS** (`nativeZoomScales` → `{x, y}`, stored as `zoom.scale` /
  `zoom.scaleY`, written `scale(sx, sy)`). `fitCanvasesToCells` floors the canvas
  box to whole CSS px, so its aspect is a hair off the video's and one uniform
  scale resamples the other axis. A fractional CSS height does NOT fix it
  (Chrome lays out in 1/64 px). `zoom.scaleY` exists only while `zoom.native`
  (`_scaleY(z)` reads it), so every other zoom is unchanged. `_rotatedBBox` /
  `_constrainOffsets` / `zoomToRect` and the rotated-view path of
  `InteractionManager.canvasToVideo` read it.
- **A pan on the device-pixel grid** (`_alignNativeOffsets`, run by
  `applyZoom` while native): what must align is where the transform puts the
  canvas's top-left — `off + c − R·c`.
Exact at an integer devicePixelRatio (and, headless, 1.25 and 1.5). At e.g.
dpr 2.2 Chrome resamples whatever the transform says, so the video is shown at
its native SIZE but not pixel-exact; `tests/e2e/native-resolution-zoom.mjs`
asserts only the size at fractional DPRs.

How it behaves:
- **Derived, not latched.** `state.nativeResolution` is true exactly when every
  shown view (`_zoomableViews`: laid out and `isViewShown`) has `zoom.native`;
  `_syncNativeMode` recomputes it after every zoom change and every pane
  rebuild (`refreshPaneInteractions`), so the menu tick cannot disagree with
  the screen.
- **Held across everything that moves the 1:1 scale** — it depends on the box
  and on devicePixelRatio. `reapplyZoom` re-derives it about the cell centre
  for a native view (cell resize, `reapplyAllZoom` in
  `loading/session-loader.js` on a DPR change). A pane rebuild KEEPS each
  view's zoom, except that while the mode is on a view coming on screen joins
  it (so cycling the solo camera keeps 1:1). A view's first zoom state
  (`initZoom`) also starts flagged while the mode is on.
- **OFF clears every view**, shown or hidden: a hidden view keeps its zoom
  through a pane rebuild, so a stale flag would bring one back at 1:1. ON flags
  hidden views for the same reason.
- **The snap.** `zoomVideo` passes each step through `snapZoomToNative`: a step
  that crosses the view's 1:1 scale, or ends within `NATIVE_SNAP_TOL` (2%) of
  it, lands exactly on it (`zoom.native` set) and returns true; a step from 1:1
  leaves it. The `[fit, 10x]` range stretches to include 1:1. The wheel
  handler adds a HOLD: after a snap, same-direction wheel events are swallowed
  until the wheel rests `NATIVE_SNAP_HOLD_MS` (250 ms), so a fast spin or
  trackpad momentum stops at 1:1; a reversal is never held. `+` / `-` snap
  with no hold. Box zoom never snaps.
- **The pane badge** (`zoomPercentLabel`, set by `applyZoom`, which also
  toggles `.video-cell.native-resolution`): only a view HELD at 1:1 reads
  exactly "100%"; a near miss is still resampled, so it reads "99.8%" / "≈100%".
- **Hovering the badge offers presets** (`ZOOM_PRESET_PERCENTS`, plus Fit; the
  menu is built in `ui/sessions-panes.js`), per pane, about the cell centre:
  `setZoomPercent(view, 100)` is `_enterNative` (exact, held — never a plain
  `scale = 1:1`); any other value is a plain uniform zoom of `pct/100 ×` the
  1:1 scale. A preset is NOT clamped to the wheel's range, and the wheel's
  range stretches to wherever the view already is
  (`lo = min(fit-min, 1:1, current)`, `hi = max(10, 1:1, current)`), so a notch
  from 50% goes to 55% instead of jumping back up to fit.
- Grid views are each put at their own 1:1, centred.

**Key exports.**
- `videoLog(msg, level)` — namespaced logger.
- `STEP_CURSOR_IDLE_MS` (3000) — idle time before a paused-stepping stream closes.
- `STEP_BACK_CHUNK` (24) — frames decoded and cached per backward step.
- `STEP_BACK_WARM_MS` (250) — pause after landing on a frame before warming the chunk behind it.
- `snapZoomToNative(oldScale, newScale, nativeScale)` → `{scale, snapped}` — the
  pure snap rule. `NATIVE_SNAP_TOL` (0.02), `NATIVE_SNAP_HOLD_MS` (250).
- `zoomPercentLabel(scale, nativeScale, isNative)` → the pane badge's read-out
  ("125%", "100%", "≈100%", or "Zoomed" when no 1:1 scale is known).
- `ZOOM_PRESET_PERCENTS` (`[50, 100, 150, 200]`) — the badge menu's presets.
- `zoomRatioLabel(pct)` — a percentage as screen:video pixels in lowest terms
  (`200` → `"2:1"`, `150` → `"3:2"`), the hint beside each preset.
- `FRAME_CACHE_BUDGET_MB` (2560), `FRAME_CACHE_MAX` (60), `FRAME_CACHE_MIN` (12),
  `frameCacheFrames(budgetMB, frameBytes[])` — the decoded-frame cache budget
  (see below).
- `releaseViewsFrames(views)` — `releaseFrames()` on every view's decoder, for a
  whole SESSION going off screen (`loading/session-loader.js`, when another
  session is loaded over it). `_shownViews` only sees the active session's
  views, so without it each loaded session kept its full caches: on 3 open
  8-camera sessions, 3.1 GB of bitmaps for the two not shown, and Track All on
  the third took 79–96 s instead of ~24 s, mostly in back-to-back full GCs
  (V8 counts the bitmaps as external memory). Test:
  `tests/test-release-views-frames.mjs`.
- `scaledBitmap(src, width)` — an ImageBitmap / VideoFrame / `<video>` as a new
  ImageBitmap `width` px wide, aspect kept (full size when not smaller).
- `OnDemandVideoDecoder` — class. Selected methods: `init(source)`,
  `getFrame(frameIndex)` (mediabunny: via `_mbGetFrame`),
  `peekFrame(frameIndex, width)` (a caller-owned copy scaled to `width`, for the
  seekbar's hover thumbnail, `ui/seekbar-preview.js` — it touches NOTHING
  playback or stepping uses: no `_mbSeekLock`, step cursor, back-prefetch,
  cache write or playback-`<video>` seek. mediabunny: a frame the backend
  holds is copied with `Map.get`, so its LRU order is untouched; any other is
  `sink.getSample(_frameTimes[i])` on its own short-lived decoder, from its
  keyframe — ~40–70 ms on a 1280×1024 60 fps HEVC field recording. No backend
  (or it fails): `_peekHTML5`, a second private `<video>` (`_peekEl`, its
  seeks serialized by `_peekLock`) on the same File / URL, seeked to
  `html5SeekTime`; re-pointed when `switchSource` changes the file, released
  by `close`. `EmbeddedVideoDecoder.peekFrame` is the same contract over
  `getFrame`, null for a frame not embedded), `releaseStepCursor()`,
  `releaseFrames()` (closes and drops every cached decoded frame — backend cache
  + HTML5/WebCodecs cache — and the stepping streams, keeping the decoder open;
  called by `VideoController._shownViews` for a view that went off screen, by
  `releaseViewsFrames` for a session that went off screen, and by `switchSession`
  for a decoder going into the cold reserve),
  `setCacheSize(n)` (re-sizes both frame caches, evicting at once — the
  backend's frames farthest from the last stepped frame first),
  `_initMediabunny(source)` / `_mediabunnyEnabled()`,
  `_mbCannotDecode(backend)` (null, or `{reason: 'codec', codec, codecString}`;
  the backend is then dropped and that object kept as `_mbUnavailable`),
  `html5SeekTime(i)` (`(i + 0.5)/fps`), `decodeRange(start, end)`,
  `playNative`, `pauseNative`, `seekNative`, `switchSource`, `close`,
  `drawCurrentFrame`, `_awaitPlayable` (init and switchSource await the
  element's load through it: when the browser refuses the file it throws
  `video-codec-diagnosis.js`'s explanation instead of a bare "Video error code
  4", with `err.codecDiagnosis` and `err.cause`), `captureCurrentFrame`
  (`{frame: VideoFrame, index}` for the per-refresh loop — index from the
  captured frame's own timestamp; caller closes the frame; null when
  WebCodecs/data is unavailable).
  `_initMp4box` reads the container's sample table for the real frame count
  and fps WITHOUT a WebCodecs support check (it never decodes with WebCodecs;
  an early return there left `_fps` at the 30 fps placeholder).
  **A fragmented MP4 is read to its end** (campy writes them by default): its
  samples are in the fragments (`moof`/`trun`), its moov says duration 0, and
  mp4box fires `onReady` on the moov, so stopping there counts only the first
  chunk's fragments. When `isFragmentedInfo(info)` the scan goes on, jumping
  over each `mdat` payload (`_nextFragmentOffset`, from mp4box's internal
  `parsingMdat`; an 8 KiB `FRAGMENT_PROBE_SIZE` read after a long jump), then
  `flush()`es; the fps comes from the sample durations and
  `videoTrack.duration` becomes their sum. `createFile(false)` drops media
  bytes — which is why the jump is required: handed the rest of an mdat whose
  start it dropped, mp4box asks for that offset again forever, so the loop
  throws if it stops advancing. A regular MP4 still stops at `onReady`. **A
  file cut off mid-write** can end inside its last fragment, whose moof lists
  frames past the end: `samplesInFile` drops them and `_frameLimit` holds
  mediabunny's count to the same number. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/fragmented-mp4-load.mjs`.
- `isFragmentedInfo(info)` — whether mp4box's `onReady` info is a fragmented MP4
  (`isFragmented`, or a video track with duration 0).
- `samplesInFile(samples, fileSize)` — the mp4box samples whose bytes lie
  inside the file (drops a truncated last fragment's missing frames).
- `sumSampleDurations(samples)` — sum of mp4box sample durations (track
  timescale units).
- `PlaybackSchedule` — class; `update(now, capturedIdx, framesPerMs)` → the
  frame index that should be on screen this refresh; `reset()`.
- `pickScheduledFrame(target, shownIdx, pendingIdx, capIdx)` →
  `'shown'|'pending'|'cap'|null`.
- `CoalescedFrameClock` — class (pure); which frame Firefox's
  `drawImage(<video>)` paints, from its coalesced rVFC callbacks.
  `observe(now, index, expectedDisplayTime, framesPerMs, fallbackPeriodMs)`
  per callback; `frameAt(t, slackMs)` → `{index, certain, step}` (index = the
  midpoint guess when not certain; step = frames per refresh) or null while
  unknown; `reset()`. Ignores a callback that only re-reports a frame, forgets
  the clock when the frame goes back, and adopts a jump AHEAD only once the
  next callback confirms it (Firefox sometimes reports a frame ~10 ahead that
  is never painted).
- `EmbeddedVideoDecoder` — class for SLP-embedded frames. `getFrame`,
  `hasFrame`, `close`.
- `VideoController` — class. Selected methods: `seekToFrame`,
  `scrubToFrame`, `togglePlayback`, `startPlayback`, `stopPlayback`,
  `pausePlayback` (user-pause: stop + frame-accurate re-decode — mediabunny,
  or the mid-frame `<video>` seek where WebCodecs cannot decode — of the
  CURRENT frame, so every camera rests exactly on-frame with the pose overlay;
  re-decoding the current frame rather than stepping one forward avoids a
  visible jump on every pause. The play button and spacebar call this,
  internal stops call `stopPlayback`),
  `_startBufferedPlayback` / `_bufferedPlaybackEnabled`,
  `setupSeekbar`, `setupKeyboardHandlers` (NOT called by the app — the live
  transport and zoom keys, `Shift+0` included, are in `ui/ui-wiring.js`), `initZoom`, `applyZoom`,
  `zoomVideo` (returns true when the step snapped to 1:1), `resetZoom`,
  `zoomToRect`, `zoomAllVideos`, `nativeZoomScales(view)` (`{x, y}` or null
  while not laid out), `setNativeResolution(on)`, `toggleNativeResolution()`,
  `nativeResolutionActive()`, `zoomPercent(view)` (% of 1:1, or null),
  `setZoomPercent(view, pct)` (the badge presets), `reapplyZoom`,
  `resetAllZoom`, `setupZoomHandlers`.

**Only the views on screen are decoded, played and captured** —
`_shownViews()`: views with a decoder for which the optional
`callbacks.isViewShown(view)` is true (the app passes `isViewDocked`, from
`ui/app-state.js`), or every decoder view when there is no callback or none is
shown. A decoder that was shown at the previous call and is not now gets
`releaseFrames()` — once, on the transition, compared BY DECODER because
decoders are pooled across session switches; otherwise the hidden views' caches
stay full and solo playback spends its time in GC. Returning to the grid costs
one keyframe decode per view. `seekToFrame` (so every step, jump and the
`pausePlayback` re-decode) decodes only those; `startPlayback` seeks, plays and
captures only those, and its loops RESTART playback when the shown set changes
mid-play (a newly shown view's `<video>` is paused wherever it was left).
Covered by `tests/test-video-controller.js` ("Only shown views") and
`tests/e2e/solo-view-decodes-shown-only.mjs`.

**The decoded-frame caches share a budget** — `FRAME_CACHE_BUDGET_MB` (2560;
`window.LUCID_FRAME_CACHE_MB` overrides). Each decoder caches decoded frames as
ImageBitmaps for paused stepping (8 MB apiece at 1680×1200); a fixed 60 per
decoder on 17 cameras is ~8 GB, and Chrome then garbage-collects most of the
time. `_applyFrameCacheBudget` (run by `_shownViews` whenever the shown decoders
change) gives each SHOWN decoder `frameCacheFrames(budget, shown frame sizes)`
frames, clamped to [`FRAME_CACHE_MIN` 12, `FRAME_CACHE_MAX` 60], and each hidden
one the share it would have with every view shown (the overlay-export preview
decodes every view). E.g. 19 frames on 17 cameras at 1680×1200, 60 for one
solo'd camera or 8 cameras at 1280×1024. Under 12 frames the backward chunk
(40% of the cache, `_backChunkSize`) gets too short for held back-stepping.
`startPlayback` also `releaseFrames()` every decoder: playback never reads the
caches. Covered by `tests/test-video-controller.js` ("decoded-frame cache
budget").

**`setupZoomHandlers`'s wheel-to-zoom stands down while Alt is held.** Alt
turns the wheel into the instance-rotation control (`ui/interaction.js`
`onWheel`), whose listener is on the OVERLAY CANVAS — which does not fill the
`.video-cell` this handler is bound to. In letterbox margin no canvas is under
the cursor, so only this `e.altKey` early return keeps an Option+scroll there
from zooming the view mid-rotation. Pinned by the margin check in
`tests/e2e/alt-wheel-rotate-instance.mjs`.

**Imports from project modules.** `ui/keyboard-target.js` — the
`shouldIgnoreShortcut` guard its `setupKeyboardHandlers` keydown listener
applies; that module imports nothing itself — and
`loading/video-codec-diagnosis.js` (`diagnoseUnplayableVideo`, for
`_awaitPlayable`) — and `loading/cold-read-guard.js`: `_initMediabunny` builds
the frame-accurate backend with `MediaBunnyVideoBackend.fromRangeSource` over
`guardedRangeSource(file)` (falling back to `fromBlob` when a bundle lacks it),
and `readChunk` (mp4box's index scan) reads through `readBlobRange`, so both
retry a failed read and re-read zeroed pages of a cloud file still downloading
(see that module and CLAUDE.md). `mediabunny` (`EncodedPacketSink`, for
`getKeyPacket`) is imported lazily by `_keyTimestamp`. Otherwise none (uses the global `MP4Box` from script tag, and
`window.SleapIO.MediaBunnyVideoBackend`).

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js`, `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `loading/session-loader.js`,
`ui/sessions-panes.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`.

**User-facing features.** All video playback (play/pause/seek/scrub),
zoom-in-on-rectangle, multi-view zoom sync, frame-accurate stepping,
keyboard transport.

## import-export/

### import-export/visibility-metadata.js

**Purpose.** The `metadata.lucid` ↔ `Session` mapping for the Visibility panel's
SESSION-SCOPED settings — and for the other per-session state that belongs in
the project file: the ID Switches tab's review checklist (`idSwitchReview`,
`ui/id-switch-review.js`) and the number of animals tracking uses
(`numAnimals` ↔ `Session.numAnimals`, the toolbar's Animals field). One module
owns the key list so a writer and a reader cannot drift: adding a setting means
touching this file and nothing else.

**`numAnimals`.** A positive integer or `"auto"`. A never-asked session
(`null`) writes nothing, so it is asked again after a reopen exactly as a new
session is, and anything else read back (0, 2.5, `"3"`, `"AUTO"`) is treated
the same way. The read assigns unconditionally, so a file without the key
leaves `null`, never a stale value. The two helpers (`serializeNumAnimals` /
`ingestNumAnimals`) are private to this module rather than in
`pose/tracker.js`, which this module cannot import: tracker.js pulls in the
whole UI, and this module is bridged into `tests/test-runner.html`, which must
not load the app.

**What it covers, and what it deliberately does not.**
- **Session-scoped** (handled here) — per-camera video brightness / contrast /
  rotation and the timeline's hidden camera / track / identity sets: they
  describe *this project's* videos and entities.
- **Global appearance preferences** — the User / Predictions / Reprojections /
  Planes / Display Legend / 3D Viewer settings — stay in
  `localStorage.visibilitySettings` (see `ui/ui-wiring.js`) on purpose: baking
  them into the `.slp` would make opening a colleague's project silently
  reassign your display settings.

**Key exports.**
- `VISIBILITY_METADATA_KEYS` — every key this module may write
  (`videoBrightness`, `videoContrast`, `videoRotation`, `hiddenCameras`,
  `hiddenTracks`, `hiddenIdentities`, `idSwitchReview`, `numAnimals`). Exported so tests can assert a default
  project carries none of them without duplicating the list.
- `writeVisibilityMetadata(lucid, session)` — mutate a `metadata.lucid` dict in
  place, adding only non-default settings; returns the same dict.
- `readVisibilityMetadata(session, lucid)` — read them all back onto a session.

**Three invariants the callers depend on.**
1. **Defaults are never written**, so a project nobody adjusted is
   byte-identical to one saved before these settings existed — pinned by
   `tests/e2e/save-golden-digest.mjs`.
2. **Only `lucid` is touched.** The writer reads nothing else off the session's
   metadata, so it cannot disturb `sessionName` / `tracks` / `frameIdentityMap` /
   `identities` / `skeleton` / `identityId` or any future sibling.
3. **Reads tolerate absence and garbage.** Every key is optional; a `.slp`
   from before this, or from SLEAP or another tool, loads unchanged. Nothing
   here throws on a malformed payload.

Purely additive to the file format (opaque JSON to sleap-io / sleap-io.js), so
files stay readable by the SLEAP GUI.

**Imports from project modules.** `../ui/video-filters.js`,
`../ui/timeline-visibility.js`, `../ui/id-switch-review.js` — all dependency-free leaves, so this module
bridges into the test runners without pulling `app.js` in.

**Imported by.** The four writers — `import-export/file-io.js`
(`buildSlpLabelsAllViews`), `import-export/slp-streaming-write.js`
(`buildSessionRefGraph`), and `import-export/save-load.js` (`saveProject`, both
the v2 and v3 project-JSON shapes) — and the four readers —
`import-export/slp-import.js` (`handleLoadSlpFile`), `loading/session-loader.js`
(`handleLoadProjectSlpLazy` and `handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp`), and
`import-export/save-load.js` (`_restoreProjectV2`).

**Tests.** `tests/test-visibility-metadata.js` (unit; bridged as
`window.__VisibilityMetadata`),
`tests/e2e/visibility-settings-roundtrip.mjs` (real app, both writers) and
`tests/e2e/reopen-folder-session-settings.mjs` (the single-SLP folder loader).

### import-export/frame-identity-metadata.js

**Purpose.** How `Session.frameIdentityMap` is stored in a `.slp`'s
`metadata.lucid`, and how it is rebuilt on load. After Track All the map holds
one entry per grouped 2D detection, each equal to its group's identity, which
the file already stores per group; written in full it was 101 MB of JSON
(3,458,373 entries) on an 8-camera, 176,406-frame project — built and parsed in
the renderer, and as one HDF5 string headed for h5wasm's ~0.45 GB string-read
ceiling on longer recordings. The writers now store only the exceptions:

- `frameIdentityMap` — entries the groups do not reproduce (same shape as before);
- `frameIdentityMapFromGroups: true` — rebuild the rest from the instance groups;
- `frameIdentityMapDropped` — keys the groups imply that the map did not hold
  (omitted when empty).

The reader derives from the groups (`stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups`), deletes
the dropped keys, then sets the listed entries; the result is the saved map
whatever order the groups are walked in (see `Session.frameIdentityExceptions`).

**Rules.** (1) Never larger than the full map: when exceptions + dropped are not
fewer than the map's entries (no groups, or a map that disagrees with them), the
full list is written without the marker — the old form. An empty map writes `[]`
as before. (2) A reader runs AFTER the groups are rebuilt. (3) The project JSON
keeps the full map. A LUC3D build from before this opens a compact file with only
the listed entries (its groups still know their animal).

**Key exports.**
- `FRAME_IDENTITY_METADATA_KEYS` — the three keys.
- `writeFrameIdentityMetadata(lucid, session)` — fill a `metadata.lucid` dict.
- `readFrameIdentityMetadata(session, lucid, opts)` — async; rebuild the map from
  a compact file; returns false (and touches nothing) for a full-form file.
- `stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups(session, opts)` — async; moved here from
  `slp-import.js` (re-exported there). Fills the map with (frame, camera, member
  track) → the group's identity for every tracked member, never overwriting an
  entry and skipping trackless members; reads `identityId` + `trackIdx` only (no
  2D hydration); yields every 40 ms with a progress message.

**Imports from project modules.** None (Session methods are reached through the
instance), so it sits outside the slp-import / session-loader import graph.

**Imported by.** Writers: `import-export/file-io.js` (`buildSlpLabelsAllViews`),
`import-export/slp-streaming-write.js` (`buildSessionRefGraph`). Readers:
`import-export/slp-import.js` (`restoreGroupingAndUnlink`),
`loading/session-loader.js` (`handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`).

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/frame-identity-compact-roundtrip.mjs`;
`tests/e2e/lazy-reopen-group-identities.mjs` (the group-only campy case).

### import-export/smoothing-metadata.js

**Purpose.** Temporal Smoothing's state in the project file, so a reopened
project is exactly as smoothed as the saved one: button and last scope lit, a
re-run replaces the last run (filters the source, not its output), windows 0
restores the 3D from before smoothing. DOM-free.

**What is stored** (only while something is smoothed, so a never-smoothed
project saves byte-identically — `save-golden-digest.mjs`):
- per group, its source (`unsmoothedPoints3d`): the writers set
  `Instance3D.lucidUnsmoothed`, the vendored writer appends it to
  `/session_data/unsmoothed_points_3d` (N x 3 f64, gzip), ROW-PARALLEL to
  `points_3d` with NaN rows for groups that have none, created only once a group
  has one (LUCID local patch (smoothing source)); the vendored reader returns
  it per group (all-NaN rows = none) and `adoptSmoothingSource` re-marks it;
- per session, `metadata.lucid.temporalSmoothing` = `{scope, medianWindow,
  gaussianWindow, maxGap, minSegment}` of the last run (the windows are
  informational; a re-run uses the current Settings).

A full copy, not "re-triangulate on demand": the source is not reproducible from
the 2D when the 3D was imported (campy-live), the triangulation settings or the
calibration changed, and a re-solve would cost a Triangulate All on reopen. Cost
is about one more `points_3d`: ~0.89 of its raw size after gzip (191 MB raw on
the 5-camera, 180,210-frame project). The state still lapses per group as
before: the mark is keyed by the `points3d` array, which every re-solve or edit
replaces.

**Key exports.** `SMOOTHING_METADATA_KEYS`; `noteSmoothingRun(session, scope,
config)` / `smoothingRunOf(session)` (the last run, per session `WeakMap`);
`smoothingSourceFor(group)` (writers); `adoptSmoothingSource(session, frameIdx,
group, instance3d)` (readers, after `points3d` is final: marks, sets
`group.smoothed`, registers the frame); `writeSmoothingMetadata(lucid,
session)`; `readSmoothingMetadata(session, lucid)`.

**Imports.** `pose/pose-data.js` (`unsmoothedPoints3d`, `markPoints3dSmoothed`,
`pooledPoints3d`), `pose/temporal-smoothing.js` (`hasSmoothed3d`,
`noteSmoothedFrames`).

**Imported by.** Writers `import-export/file-io.js`,
`import-export/slp-streaming-write.js`, `import-export/save-load.js` (project
JSON: `unsmoothedPoints3d` per group, the key at the session-dict level).
Readers `import-export/slp-import.js` (both typed group rebuilders +
`handleLoadSlpFile`), `loading/session-loader.js` (lazy reopen, single-SLP
folder), `import-export/save-load.js`. `ui/smoothing-button.js`;
`ui/ui-wiring.js` (`smoothingRunOf`: the scope Remove smoothing keeps).

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/smoothing-save-reopen.mjs` (eager + lazy reopen, both
writers, two sessions, an edit after reopen, a never-smoothed project).

### import-export/mesh-export.js

**Purpose.** A 3D mesh object as a file another program can open: **binary
STL** for CAD and **binary glTF (.glb)** for Blender. Both take the same
`MeshObjectGeometry` that `pose/mesh-object-geometry.js` derives, so neither
format re-derives anything and a shape that is wrong in one is wrong in both
rather than wrong in different ways. DOM-free and pure — geometry in, bytes
out; `ui/mesh-objects.js` owns the buttons and the download.

**The one sanctioned axis conversion.** LUCID's world is Z-up, Blender's is
too, and no axis conversion belongs in the plane/mesh pipeline — but glTF's
spec FIXES the up axis at +Y (glTF 2.0 3.5):

| format | axes | why |
|---|---|---|
| STL | verbatim Z-up | the format defines no up axis; CAD treats Z as up |
| GLB | `(x, y, z)` -> `(x, z, -y)` | the spec mandates +Y up; Blender's importer applies the inverse, landing it back Z-up |

Write Z-up into a .glb instead and the file is off-spec: Blender still rotates
it, so the cage arrives on its side, and every conformant viewer shows it
tipped over. The conversion is a -90 degree rotation about X, determinant +1 —
a rotation, **not** a mirror — so triangle winding survives and **no winding
flip accompanies it**. Adding one later would silently invert every normal.

**Key exports.**
- `meshObjectToSTL(geometry, {name})` -> `Uint8Array | null`. Binary, not
  ASCII: ~5x smaller and exact to the last float32 bit. The 80-byte header
  deliberately does not begin with `solid`, which is what makes lenient parsers
  guess ASCII and read garbage.
- `meshObjectToGLB(geometry, {name, color})` -> `Uint8Array | null`. One
  self-contained file (JSON chunk + BIN chunk, spec padding: spaces for JSON,
  zeros for BIN). Vertices stay **welded** and indexed. **No NORMAL attribute,
  deliberately** — glTF then mandates flat shading, right for a faceted cage;
  supplying normals would mean splitting every shared vertex (undoing the
  welding) or smoothing across real hard edges. The colour is converted sRGB ->
  **linear**, because `baseColorFactor` is linear and passing the picker's hex
  through makes every export paler than the swatch.
- `isExportable(geometry)` — what the buttons gate on.
- `meshFilenameStem(name)` — object names are free text, and a slash in a
  download name is a path the browser will not write.

**Both return `null` rather than an empty file**, which would open, show
nothing, and read as "my annotation is broken".

**Precision.** Positions are float32 in both formats (what the formats define),
so a re-imported mesh is not bit-identical to the project's float64 numbers.
The project file stays the source of truth.

**Imports from project modules.** None. **Imported by.** `ui/mesh-objects.js`.

**Tests.** `tests/test-mesh-export.mjs` (the byte layouts, with a negative
control on each axis claim) and `tests/e2e/mesh-object-export.mjs` (the buttons
and the downloaded bytes). The axis behaviour was also confirmed against real
Blender (both files put the tracked corner at (0, 0, 300)).

---

### import-export/plane-metadata.js

**Purpose.** The `metadata.lucid` ↔ plane-state mapping. The Define Planes
pipeline (nodes, planes, per-view 2D, triangulation, plane fit, origin frame) is
project state, so it is saved, and plane edits mark the project dirty. Same
shape and the same three invariants as `import-export/visibility-metadata.js`;
one module owns the key list so a writer and a reader cannot drift.

**The scope split, and why one payload is written N times.** `metadata.lucid` is
per SESSION, but the plane model is not:
- The **node pool, the planes, the 3D Mesh Objects and the origin frame are
  PROJECT-scoped** — a node has one 3D position whatever session you are
  looking at. They are written IDENTICALLY into every session's dict, so
  opening any one session of a multi-session project restores the same
  geometry, and read back by whichever session is ingested first. The
  duplication is a few dozen nodes, not a frame table.
- The **per-view 2D is SESSION-scoped**, because a view belongs to a session. It
  lives on `Session.planePlacements`, exactly where `planeModel()` picks it up.

**Key exports.**
- `PLANE_METADATA_KEYS` — `planeNodes`, `planes`, `meshObjects`,
  `planePlacements`, `planeOrigin`. Exported so the slim-metadata / golden-digest
  guards can assert a project that never opened the feature carries none of them.
- `writePlaneMetadata(lucid, session)` — mutate a `metadata.lucid` dict in place;
  returns the same dict. Reads the pool / planes off the module singleton and the
  2D off `session.planePlacements`, so saving a BACKGROUND session writes its own
  placements rather than whichever map the model happens to have attached.
- `readPlaneMetadata(session, lucid)` — placements land on `session`; the
  project-scoped half lands on the shared model **only when it is still empty**.
  That guard makes ingesting N sessions idempotent (appending would double
  every node).
- `resetPlaneState()` — empty the model (pool, planes AND mesh objects, via one
  `restorePlaneProject(model, null)`), drop the applied origin and the plane
  selection. **Every load path must call it**: given the empty-model guard,
  skipping it keeps the OLD project's planes and silently discards the NEW
  one's. Called by `newProject`, the JSON load, the eager `.slp` import, the
  lazy reopen, the demo-session load, and the single-SLP folder load when no
  session is open.
- `planeMetadataConflict(lucid)` — why a `metadata.lucid` dict's plane state
  cannot be read into the live model, or `null` when it can. For a loader that
  ADDS a session to an open project (the single-SLP folder load) and so does not
  reset: the file's 2D is seated on the open pool by node ID, and plane-node IDs
  restart at 1 in every project, so another project's corners would land on
  this one's nodes. `null` when the model is empty, or when every node and plane
  the file lists is in the model under the same id AND name; otherwise a short
  reason for the status bar.

**What is deliberately NOT written.** The plane panel's node size, edge width and
3D corner size (browser-local display taste) and the editor's transient
selections. The one thing restored beyond the data is the plane SELECTION being
re-pointed at a plane that still exists, since a selection naming a deleted
plane renders an empty editor.

**Imports from project modules.** `../pose/plane-serialization.js` (all the
actual mapping); `../ui/plane-definition.js` — `planeState`;
`../ui/origin-definition.js` — `originState`; `../ui/app-state.js` — `state`.
The two `ui/` imports are circular by design and safe: every use is inside a
function body, so the bindings resolve at call time.

**Imported by.** The four writers — `import-export/file-io.js`
(`buildSlpLabelsAllViews`), `import-export/slp-streaming-write.js`
(`buildSessionRefGraph`), and `import-export/save-load.js` (`saveProject`, both
the v2 and v3 project-JSON shapes) — and the four readers —
`import-export/slp-import.js` (`handleLoadSlpFile`), `loading/session-loader.js`
(`handleLoadProjectSlpLazy` and `handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp`), and
`import-export/save-load.js` (`_restoreProjectV2`). Plus `pose/initialization.js` for `resetPlaneState` on the
demo-session load.

**Tests.** `tests/e2e/plane-persistence-roundtrip.mjs` (real app, both `.slp`
writers, dirty flag, scope split, untouched-project and replace-not-merge
controls), `tests/test-plane-serialization.mjs` (the mapping) and
`tests/e2e/reopen-folder-session-settings.mjs` (the single-SLP folder load,
incl. `planeMetadataConflict`).

### import-export/slp-provenance.js

**Purpose.** Keeps a loaded `.slp`'s `provenance` dict through LUC3D's project
saves. Other programs keep real records there — campy-live-export
(olinesn/campy `campy/liveexport.py` `Provenance()`) stores the recording's
session.json, its campy-live-session.json, `animalIds` and `calibrationDiffers`
— and every save used to replace it with a fresh `{ source: 'lucid',
exported_at }`. DOM-free.

**The rule.** Every key the file had is written back unchanged; LUC3D's
`source: 'lucid'` + `exported_at` are refreshed and only ever replace LUC3D's
own earlier pair (another program's `source`/`exported_at` stay, and LUC3D's
pair goes under `lucid`). Sessions from different files: the first session
with a provenance gives the top level, every session whose provenance differs
is kept whole under `lucid_sessions` by session name (`{}` = none; a repeated
name is `name #2`, …), and goes back to that session on reopen. No provenance
anywhere → exactly `{ source, exported_at }`. The 2D exports (one camera,
multi-session export) do not use it: they keep `{ source, exported_at }`.

**Key exports.** `buildSaveProvenance(sessions, exportedAt?)` (reads each
session's `name` + `fileProvenance`); `fileSessionProvenance(fileProv, names,
index)` (what one loaded session keeps); `fileSessionNames(rawSessions)`
(`metadata.lucid.sessionName` per `sessions_json` entry);
`foreignProvenance(prov)` (the dict minus LUC3D's keys, deep-copied);
`provenanceSessionKeys(names)`; `LUCID_PROVENANCE_SOURCE`,
`LUCID_PROVENANCE_KEY` (`lucid`), `PROVENANCE_SESSIONS_KEY` (`lucid_sessions`).

**Imports from project modules.** None.

**Imported by.** Writers: `import-export/save-load.js` (`buildSlpBytes` eager
and single-session streaming, `finalizeMultiSessionSave`). Readers (set
`session.fileProvenance`): `import-export/slp-import.js` (`handleLoadSlpFile`),
`loading/session-loader.js` (`handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp`,
`handleLoadProjectSlpLazy`). A new loader that builds a Session from an `.slp`
must set it too, or a save drops the file's provenance. The per-camera and
multi-session folder loaders do not (they read camera `.slp` files as 2D only).

**Tests.** `tests/test-slp-provenance.mjs` (the rule),
`tests/e2e/slp-provenance-roundtrip.mjs` (real load → Save → reopen: eager,
lazy single-session and multi-session streaming writers, two sessions, a file
without provenance, a ~120 KB provenance).
