### import-export/skeleton-json.js

**Purpose.** Pure, DOM-free (de)serialization for standalone `.skeleton.json`
files in the SLEAP jsonpickle node-link format. Split out of `ui/info-panel.js`
(which keeps only the download / file-picker wrappers) so the round-trip logic is
unit-testable without a browser.

**Key exports.**
- `buildSkeletonJSON(skeleton)` — returns the skeleton-JSON object (no I/O). Emits
  each node's full `py/object` (carrying its name) exactly once at first
  occurrence: in `links` if the node has an edge, otherwise in the `nodes` array.
  Without that, **edgeless nodes** lose their names on re-import and come back
  as `node_<i>`.
- `parseSkeletonJSON(jsonText)` — parses jsonpickle Format 1, plus the simpler
  `{skeleton:{…}}` and direct node/edge-array formats; returns a `Skeleton` or
  null. Accepts SLEAP's **list-wrapped** GUI export (`[{…}]`) and the `nx_graph`
  nesting as well as a bare object.

**Reading SLEAP's own exports.** The jsonpickle branch (module-private
`parseJsonPickleSkeleton`) reconstructs jsonpickle's `py/id` memo table, and
the two writers in the wild disagree about it:

- SLEAP / `sleap_io` re-emit a node's **full `py/object` at every appearance** in
  `links` and use `py/id` only in `nodes`, so a node takes ONE memo slot however
  many edges it touches.
- `buildSkeletonJSON` emits the full object once, then `py/id` back-references.

Deduplicating by name satisfies both. Counting every `py/object` gives a SLEAP
export with a hub node bogus slots, and since its `nodes` array is nothing but
`py/id` references, every later node resolves wrong — silently.

Node **order** has its own wrinkle: SLEAP's encoder numbers node `py/id`s without
counting the EdgeType's slot while its decoder counts it, so the `nodes` array is
off by one from the table it nominally indexes. `sleap_io` gives up and returns
edge-traversal order; we re-read the array in the node-only space, recovering the
order SLEAP actually displays — so the JSON path agrees with the `.slp` path.
Both interpretations must yield a complete permutation to be accepted, else we
fall back to traversal order as `sleap_io` does. Edge types are resolved against
the shared table first and SLEAP's **separate** edge-type id space (1 = regular,
2 = symmetry) only on a miss; symmetries are dropped, their nodes kept.

**Imports from project modules.** `../pose/pose-data.js` — `Skeleton`.

**Imported by.** `ui/info-panel.js`, `loading/session-loader.js`. Tested by
`tests/test-skeleton-json.js`.

### import-export/file-io.js

**Purpose.** File-picker helpers, calibration parsing (TOML + JSON),
SLP-LABELS bytes-builder used by export, points3d / reproj H5 builders,
parser stubs that spawn `slp-import-worker.js`. The "low-level" file
layer.

**Per-camera export is STREAMED on a lazy project (`exportCameraSlpStreaming`).**
The eager builders iterate `session.frameGroups` — the RESIDENT map — so on a
reopened lazy project they would silently write only the resident frames. The
lazy path does what the resident path does, one window at a time.
`_buildCameraExportHeader` (skeleton/tracks/video) and
`_buildCameraLabeledFrame` (one camera's frame) are SHARED by both paths so they
cannot drift. `exportCameraSlpStreaming` opens `SIO.openSlpWriter`, drives
`sweepLazyFrameWindows` (hydrate → build → `appendFrames` → release, 256-frame
batches), and finalizes to bytes or a sink; peak is one window regardless of
project size. Manual corrections survive because the sweep hydrates live 2D — the
case the verbatim `lazyCameraExportBytes` fast path structurally cannot handle.
Identities are deliberately NOT passed (matching `buildSlpLabels`: non-empty
`identities` bumps the format to 1.9, unreadable by sleap-io Python <= 0.6.x).
`exportSlpClientSide` and single-selection `exportSlpMultiSession` route here
whenever `_exportWouldTruncate(session)`; fully-resident projects keep the eager
path. **Coverage precondition:** `sweepLazyFrameWindows` hydrates via
`batchLoadLazyFrames`, which reads the ACTIVE `state.session` — so a non-active
session would emit a resident-only file. The exporter counts frames the sweep
offered against that camera's store rows and THROWS if short, naming the
numbers.

**Export truncation guard (`assertExportCoversProject`).** The eager builders
(`buildSlpLabels`, `buildSlpLabelsMultiSession`, `buildSlpLabelsAllViews`,
`buildPerCameraSlpJson`, `buildSlpExportData`) all iterate the resident
`session.frameGroups`. A single-selection export of a partly-resident session
goes through `lazyCameraExportBytes` (when the filter allows the verbatim
store) or else `exportCameraSlpStreaming`; a MULTI-selection
`exportSlpMultiSession` into one file (needs a multi-video writer header) calls
`assertExportCoversProject` before the eager build and THROWS (naming each
session's resident/total) rather than truncate silently. Covered by
`tests/test-lazy-export-instance-filter.js`.

**Key exports.**
- File pickers: `pickFiles`, `pickFolder`, `pickVideoFiles`.
- Calibration: `parseCalibrationTOML`, `parseCalibrationJSON`,
  `loadCalibrationFile` (→ `{cameras, fileName, file}`; `file` lets
  `handleLoadCalibration` hash it for a campy recording),
  `exportCalibrationTOML`, `downloadTOML`.
- Video matching: `matchVideosToCameras`, `buildVideoGrid`.
- SLP build: `buildSlpExportData`, `buildPerCameraSlpJson`,
  `buildSlpLabels`, `buildSlpLabelsAllViews`,
  `buildSlpLabelsMultiSession`, `serializeSkeleton`, `_buildSioPoints`
  (per-node `[x,y,visible,complete]` builder — nulled/occluded/optional
  per-point score; also reused by `slp-streaming-write.js` for edited-frame
  overlays). **A point's score is its node's OWN model score** when it has one
  (`Instance.pointScore`, or the caller's `nodeScore(k)`), and the
  `perPointScore` (the instance score) only for a node with none; with
  `nodeOrder` the canonical node gets its LOCAL node's score
  (`buildSlpLabelsAllViews` applies the same rule to its pooled columns). Pinned by `tests/test-node-score-export.js` and
  `tests/e2e/node-score-tooltip.mjs`.
  Export is raw `saveSlpToBytes`; SLEAP >= 1.6 / sleap-io >= 0.7 reads the
  flat-matrix `field_names` layout natively. On 2D export both `buildSlpLabels` and
  `buildSlpLabelsMultiSession` keep each instance's own track — grouped
  AND ungrouped/unlinked — so a flat 2D project's tracks survive; an
  ungrouped instance only drops its track if a grouped instance already
  holds that track in the same frame (SLEAP forbids two instances sharing
  a (frame, track) pair). Reprojections still export trackless.
  **Both normalize a null/undefined `videoFileInfo` to `{}`** (a lazy session
  before "Load Videos", or a calibration-only camera), degrading to the
  `cameraName + '.mp4'` / zero-dimension fallback, like
  `slp-streaming-write.js`'s `resolveVideoPath`.
- SLP export (client-side): `exportSlpClientSide`,
  `exportSlpMultiSession`, `exportCameraSlpStreaming` (above). For a **lazy session** these route a plain
  per-camera export through `lazyCameraExportBytes` → `saveSlpToBytes` on the
  camera's already-lazy `Labels` (all frames, memory-bounded) instead of the
  eager `buildSlpLabels*`. The multi-view project save uses the streaming
  writer via `slp-streaming-write.js` (see `save-load.js` / `buildSlpBytes`).
  The fast path is gated on `instanceFilterAllowsLazyFastPath(instanceFilter)`,
  NOT a literal `!instanceFilter`: every export-modal call site
  (`ui/export-modals.js`) builds a non-null `instanceFilter` even at default
  settings. It runs whenever the filter needs nothing the verbatim store cannot
  provide (no reprojections, predicted/user not excluded).
  **Data-safety guard (never silently drop a manual correction):**
  `lazyCameraExportBytes` re-emits the RAW columnar store verbatim, with no
  notion of a live-edited `Instance` — so it checks every resident frame via a
  local `frameGroupHasUserInstances` (copied from the identical helper in
  `pose/tracker.js`/`ui/export-modals.js`) and returns `null` if ANY resident
  frame carries a user-type instance; the caller then takes the streaming (or,
  fully resident, eager) correction-aware path. Covered by
  `tests/test-lazy-export-instance-filter.js`.
- `buildSlpLabelsAllViews` builds the full typed graph (RecordingSession /
  FrameGroup / InstanceGroup with `instance3d`, `identity`, and `metadata.lucid`)
  that `saveSlpToBytes` serializes as **SLP 2.8**: 3D points + grouping go to the
  columnar `/session_data` group and `sessions_json` stays slim (calibration +
  video map + session metadata + frame-group range). It writes each session's
  identity list into `metadata.lucid.identities` AND each group's per-session
  index into `InstanceGroup.metadata.lucid.identityId` (authoritative on
  reload — the canonical `identity_idx`/`ig.identity` resolve against the
  file-level concat and mis-scope for multi-session files). The file-level
  `identities_json` is a cross-session concatenation, NOT the per-session
  source of truth on reload.
  **Its 2D instances come from `makePooledSioInstances(SIO, skeleton, numNodes)`**
  (exported), not `new SIO.Instance({ points: _buildSioPoints(...) })` (four
  typed arrays per instance, held for the whole save — about 4x the heap). The
  factory writes the same values into pooled column chunks
  (`SIO_POOL_CHUNK_POINTS` = 65,536 keypoints) and returns
  `Object.create(prototype)` with `track`, `score`, its chunk and offset; the
  prototype inherits from the real `SIO.Instance` / `SIO.PredictedInstance`,
  copies every field default from a real `new` instance (so a field a future
  sleap-io.js adds keeps its default), and serves `_xy` / `_visible` /
  `_complete` / `_score` as getters returning a view of the slice — what the
  vendored writer's `emitInstancePointsInto` reads. A `points =` assignment
  gives that one instance its own columns. Saved bytes are bit-identical;
  pinned against the plain construction (every column, score, default, and the
  saved bytes) by `tests/test-sio-pooled-instances.js`.
- Skeleton validation: `findSkeletonMismatch(selections)` — returns `null` when
  all selected sessions share a skeleton (node count + names, in order),
  otherwise a human-readable mismatch message. Pure (no SleapIO); used both to
  guard `buildSlpLabelsMultiSession` and to pre-flight the per-camera download.
- SLP parse (raw worker): `parseSlpH5(file, onProgress, opts)` — spawns
  `slp-import-worker.js`. Kept for SLEAP analysis `.h5`, as the
  `parseSlpViaSleapIO` fallback, and for the per-camera session-folder load,
  which passes `{ columnar: true }` to receive the poses as flat transferred
  typed arrays (`data.columnar`) instead of nested `data.frames`.
- SLP parse (sleap-io.js): `parseSlpViaSleapIO(file, onProgress)` —
  drives `window.SleapIO.readSlpStreaming(file, {rawSessions:true})` and adapts
  the typed `Labels` into the `slpData` shape via the private
  `_typedInstanceToSlpData` pose transform (columnar `_xy`/`_visible` →
  `points[]` + parallel `occluded[]`, NOT `numpy()`; a prediction's per-node
  scores as `pointScores`, the typed instance's own `_score` column by reference,
  null for a user instance — the importers copy it with `Instance.setPointScores`). Each `sessions[]` entry is
  the verbatim on-disk dict (for the direct calibration/video-map/metadata reads)
  PLUS a `_typedSession` ref (the typed RecordingSession) used by
  `reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSession` for grouping — which reads LUCID's legacy
  inline `frame_group_dicts`, the canonical `sessions_json`, and the SLP 2.8
  columnar `/session_data`. The reader's I/O worker loads the vendored h5wasm
  IIFE via `h5wasmUrl`. `provenance` is the file's whole provenance dict
  (`labels.provenance`; the raw worker returns it too), for
  `import-export/slp-provenance.js`.
- H5 build/parse: `buildPoints3dH5`, `buildReprojH5`,
  `buildPoints3dExportData`, `parsePoints3dH5`, `h5FileToBlob`. `buildReprojH5`
  (Save Reprojections) projects a group's stored 3D into each camera when it has
  no `reprojections` — they are derived, kept only for frames that have been
  shown, and Triangulate All stores none; skipping such groups would leave every
  unshown frame NaN.
- Misc: `downloadJSON`, `downloadBytes` (the binary sibling, for the STL/GLB
  mesh exports; the Blob is handed the VIEW, never `bytes.buffer`, which on a
  subarray would write the neighbouring bytes too), `instancePointsMatch`,
  `instanceMatchesPoints`
  (the same comparison with a LUCID `Instance` on the left, read through the flat
  typed accessors so the SLP-load dedup passes don't allocate a boxed points array
  per candidate).

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `Camera`, `Skeleton`, `Instance`, `Identity`,
  `toBoxedPoints3d`, `getPoint3d`, `points3dNodeCount`.
- `./slp-merge.js` — `validateSkeletonCompatibility`.
- `../pose/triangulation.js` — `getOrComputeReprojectedInstance`,
  `sweepLazyFrameWindows`, `reprojectPointsCamera`.
- `./visibility-metadata.js` — `writeVisibilityMetadata` (its only deps are
  dependency-free `ui/` leaves, so it adds nothing to this module's import
  graph).
- `./frame-identity-metadata.js` — `writeFrameIdentityMetadata` (fills
  `metadata.lucid.frameIdentityMap` in `buildSlpLabelsAllViews`; no imports).
- `./plane-metadata.js` — `writePlaneMetadata`.
- `./smoothing-metadata.js` — `writeSmoothingMetadata`, and `smoothingSourceFor`
  (each smoothed group's source rides on its `Instance3D` as `lucidUnsmoothed`).

**Imported by.** `import-export/save-load.js`,
`import-export/slp-import.js`, `import-export/slp-streaming-write.js`
(`_buildSioPoints`), `loading/session-loader.js`,
`ui/export-modals.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`, `ui/mesh-objects.js`
(`downloadBytes`), `ui/origin-definition.js`
(`exportCalibrationTOML` + `downloadTOML`) and
`ui/origin-rebase.js` (the same two, for `calibration-rebased.toml`).

**User-facing features.** Underlies File menu Load Calibration / Load
Videos / Export TOML / Export SLP / Export H5; spawns SLP-parse worker.

---

### import-export/slp-streaming-write.js

**Purpose.** Memory-bounded SLP *save* for large lazy sessions — the write-side
companion to `SioLazyLoader`. The eager builder
(`buildSlpLabelsAllViews` + `saveSlpToBytes`) materializes one `Labels` with every
frame; on a large lazy prediction session that OOMs and silently drops every
unvisited frame (it only iterates the resident `frameGroups`).

**Merged-save memory: the binding constraint is V8's ~4 GB renderer heap cap,
not WASM** (`--max-old-space-size` does not raise it; the h5wasm heap was never
the constraint). The vendored writer's `writeSessions` therefore keeps its
`/session_data` tables off the V8 heap and flushes them incrementally
(`// LUCID local patch` markers `luc3d #185`, `#190`, `#191`; documented in
`CLAUDE.md`, guarded by `tests/e2e/save-session-3d-typed-sink.mjs`).

**Multi-session two-pass split.** `SIO.openSlpWriter`
serializes `sessions_json`/`identities_json` (+ the SLP 2.8 columnar `/session_data`
group holding 3D points + grouping) **synchronously at open time** (not
at `close()`), so every session's ref-based `RecordingSession` graph — with
correct file-**global** `lf_idx`/`inst_idx` (sleap-io resolves refs against one
flat, file-wide labeled-frames table, never per-session) — must be complete
*before* the writer opens, i.e. before any frame streams. For multiple large
lazy sessions that can never all be resident at once, this forces two passes,
each holding only one session's data at a time:
- **`createProjectWriterContext()`** — a fresh shared context
  (`{ runningOut, allSkeletons, allVideos, allTracks, allIdentities,
  trackBaseByNameSig }`) threaded through every session so refs stay
  file-global (never reset per session).
- **`buildSessionRefGraph(session, views, videoFiles, ctx)`** — **`async`** (both
  call sites must `await`: `buildSessionSlpBytesStreaming` here and
  `commitSessionForMultiSessionSave` in `save-load.js`). PASS 1, per
  session: prunes `session.frameGroups` to user-edited frames only (this only
  releases the `FrameGroup` wrappers — every grouped `Instance` stays reachable
  via `session.instanceGroups`), builds the overlay plan / `storeOutIndex` /
  per-`InstanceGroup` refs against `ctx`'s running counter (advancing it for
  the next session), and accumulates this session's cameras/tracks/skeleton
  into `ctx`. Requires `session.lazyLoader` open and Track All/Triangulate All
  already run. Touches no writer. Returns `{ sioSession, overlayLfs, cam }` —
  small enough that the caller can evict the session's lazy loader/
  `frameGroups`/`instanceGroups` right after.
  **Non-shared-store track dedup:** for separate per-camera prediction files
  (session-folder load, `loader._sharedStore` false), each camera's
  `labels.tracks` is appended onto `ctx.allTracks` under a growing
  `trackOffset` — but `Session.propagateIdentitiesToTracks`
  (`pose/pose-data.js`, via `remapTracksFromIdentity`,
  `loading/sio-lazy-loader.js`) rewrites EVERY camera's `labels.tracks` to the
  SAME identity-derived list, and appending a duplicate per camera leaves only
  the first camera's frames with valid tracks. `ctx.trackBaseByNameSig` (a
  `'|~|'`-joined track-name-list signature → the `trackBase` it was first added
  at) dedups this the way `skeletonByName` dedups skeletons — a camera whose
  track list exactly matches one already added reuses that base. Covered by
  `tests/test-slp-streaming-write.js` ("propagateIdentitiesToTracks then
  export").

  Memory measures in the ref-resolution loop (once per grouped frame, the
  PASS-1 hot path):
  - **`CamRefMap`** replaces the per-`InstanceGroup` `new Map()` for
    `instanceRefsByCamera` with a two-parallel-array container (a real `Map`
    allocates a hash table regardless of size). It is **duck-typed against the
    vendored writer** — only `get`/`set`/`has`/`keys()`/`for...of` are
    implemented, which is exactly what `instanceGroupMemberRows` + the
    `InstanceGroup` constructor use as of sleap-io.js 0.5.5. **Re-check on every
    re-vendor**; a new `.size`/`.forEach`/`.values()`/`.entries()`/`.delete()`
    call would silently read `undefined`.
  - **`labeledFrameRefsByCamera` is not built**: `instanceGroupMemberRows`
    derives `lfByCamera` only when a group carries CONCRETE instances
    (`_instanceByCamera`), which LUCID's ref-based groups never set, and
    otherwise takes the pair from `instRefs.get(camera)`.
  - **`await yieldToBrowser()` every 2,000 frames** so the save progress modal
    paints and V8 gets collection points — this is why the function is `async`.
    An optional 5th argument, `onProgress`, is the Save progress card's hook:
    called (and awaited) as `onProgress('refs', done, total)` once before the
    synchronous pruning / overlay / row-map work, then at those yield points
    and at the end, over the session's grouped frames.
- **`openProjectWriter(ctx, allSioSessions, provenance)`** — the single
  `SIO.openSlpWriter(...)` call, made once every session's ref graph is final.
- **`streamSessionIntoWriter(writer, session, refGraphResult, onProgress)`** —
  **`async`** (callers must `await`). PASS 2, per
  session: `appendFrames(overlayLfs)` + `appendStore(store, {videoIndexOffset,
  trackOffset})` per camera, using the offsets saved in pass 1. Requires
  `session.lazyLoader` to be (re)opened — no Track All/Triangulate All needed;
  `appendStore` reads straight from the columnar store (a far lower peak than
  pass 1). With `onProgress` it appends through the vendored writer's
  `appendStoreAsync` instead (LUCID local patch, save progress — same rows,
  same bytes), calling and awaiting `onProgress('frames', done, total,
  'camera frames')` after each 5,000-row window, over every store row being
  appended (one row per camera per frame). On the real 8-camera,
  176,406-frame project this is the longest step of a save: 23.8–24.7 s of
  33.8–35.2 s, since the gzip of the 2D tables (`luc3d gzip`) is done here.

**Shared-store re-save (lazy project reopen).** A session reopened via
`SioLazyLoader.openProjectSlp` has `loader._sharedStore` set: ONE interleaved
store holds every camera's frames, so `streamSessionIntoWriter` appends it
ONCE (appending per camera would write duplicate frames + tracks). Two extra
pieces make this correct for arbitrary inputs:
- `buildSessionRefGraph`'s shared-store branch keys its store-row → camera map
  (`videoToCam`) on the NATIVE store video ids (`loader.videoIdByCam`), not on
  the output header order.
- `_remapSharedStoreVideos(store, headerByNative)` (module-private) +
  `streamSessionIntoWriter`: when the native ids differ from the output header
  indices (a project `.slp` written by Python sleap-io, or a multi-session
  save where this session's videos sit at a global offset), the store's
  `framesData.video` column (and the video-keyed annotation/negative-frame
  keys) is remapped onto the header indices via a shallow wrapper — only the
  video column is copied; `appendStore`'s own `buildVideoIdMap` remap is a
  no-op for external-video files, so the remapped ids written ARE final
  (`videos: []` keeps that passthrough inert). Identity maps skip the copy.

**Video fallback for video-less reopened sessions.** `resolveVideoPath(cam,
views, videoFiles, lazyVid)` and the `buildSessionRefGraph` camera loop fall
back to the reopened file's own video record (`loader.videos`: filename +
shape) when no live video is attached, so the re-save round-trips the original
video filename/shape instead of degrading to `<camName>.mp4` with zero
dimensions. Covered by `tests/test-lazy-reopen.js`.
- **`finalizeProjectWriter(writer, opts)`** — `writer.close()`/`writeToSink()`.
- **`buildSessionSlpBytesStreaming(session, views, videoFiles, opts)`** — thin
  single-session wrapper chaining all four. `opts.onProgress(stage, done,
  total, unit)` drives the Save progress card (`save-load.js`), one stage
  after another: `'refs'` (counted), `'header'` (uncounted: `/session_data`,
  written inside `openSlpWriter` without a break), `'frames'` (counted) and
  `'finish'` (uncounted: `close()` / `writeToSink()`). Every call is awaited,
  so the card paints a step before the vendored writer holds the main thread.
  Measured on the real 8-camera, 176,406-frame project (headless Chromium on
  an M-series Mac, four saves — two through the download path, two streamed
  to a picked file — at commit f7b11af7, i.e. with the gzip of #385):
  3.6–4.0 / 5.5–5.6 / 23.8–24.7 / 0.5–0.9 s, 33.8–35.2 s in all, writing
  945,024,789 bytes (901 MB). Before #385 the same project took 6.7 / 7.7 /
  13.9 / 6.1 s (36.0 s) and wrote 1.8 GB: compression moved time into
  `'frames'` and out of `'finish'`, which hands over half as many bytes.

**Calibration-only cameras** (in `session.cameras` but with no loaded lazy
store) get a header `Camera`+`Video` (0-frame) and a cameraGroup slot so the
calibration round-trips, but are skipped for `appendStore`. Loaded cameras
carry their GLOBAL `videoIndex`/`trackBase` (position in
`ctx.allVideos`/`ctx.allTracks`) into `appendStore`'s offsets and the overlay's
video ref. **2D user corrections are overlaid:** any resident camera-frame
carrying a user instance is materialized (grouped + unlinked union, via the
shared `_buildSioPoints`) and `appendFrames`d FIRST, so the writer's
first-write-wins dedup shadows the store's original predicted row. Overlays
therefore come first in output order and shadowed store rows are skipped, so
grouped frames' output indices are recomputed (`storeOutIndex`, continuing the
shared running counter) and `refFor` resolves an edited camera-frame to its
overlay (instance by object identity, then `track`) or else to the shifted
store row. An overlaid frame's untouched
predicted siblings are re-materialized too (the store row is skipped
wholesale); each node carries its OWN model score, read back from the row being
replaced (`Session.predictedNodeScore` → `SioLazyLoader.predictedPointScore`,
by the sibling's `_rawInstIndex`), and the instance score only where it has
none — a point with no score is one SLEAP's GUI hides. **Scope:** predictions +
full grouping (identities + 3D) + 2D corrections.

**Imports.** `window.SleapIO` (streaming writer API); `_buildSioPoints`
(`import-export/file-io.js`); `points3dNodeCount` (`pose/pose-data.js`);
`writeVisibilityMetadata` (`import-export/visibility-metadata.js` — must stay in
lockstep with the eager writer in `file-io.js`, which is why both call the one
helper); `writeFrameIdentityMetadata` (`import-export/frame-identity-metadata.js`,
same reason); `writePlaneMetadata` (`import-export/plane-metadata.js`, same reason);
`writeSmoothingMetadata` + `smoothingSourceFor` (`import-export/smoothing-metadata.js`,
same reason).
**Imported by.** `import-export/save-load.js`
(`buildSlpBytes`, `saveAllSessionsStreaming`, `commitSessionForMultiSessionSave`,
`finalizeMultiSessionSave`).

### import-export/save-load.js

**Purpose.** Project lifecycle — newProject, save paths (quickSave,
saveAs, saveProjectSlp, saveProject), load dispatcher
(`handleLoadProject`), session-frame serialization helpers, the
loading-overlay/status-text UI helpers.

**Key exports.**
- **The Save progress card.** `quickSave`/`saveAs` and `saveProjectSlp` (the
  download path, which is what Save is in Brave — its save dialog cannot open
  until the whole file is built) show the shared loading overlay
  (`ui/loading-overlay.js`), titled "Save", from the start of the save until it
  ends, fails or is cancelled. `createSaveProgress(steps)` (internal) is the
  `onProgress(stage, done, total, unit)` hook passed down `buildSlpBytes` into
  the streaming writer: a counted stage moves the bar (`showLoadingProgress`,
  repainting at most every `PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MS`), an uncounted one shows the
  spinner with its step (`showLoading` with `step`/`steps`), and every call
  that changes the card returns a promise for the next paint, so a step is on
  screen before the work it names. The steps (`saveSteps(streaming,
  download)`): Collecting 3D groups (counted) · Writing 3D groups and points ·
  Writing 2D poses (counted) · Finishing the file — or, for an eager project,
  Writing the file — plus Opening the save dialog on the download path.
  `progress.disk(bytes)` reports the streamed write to a picked file
  ("Writing to disk: N MB…"). `quickSave` puts the card up only after the
  save-file picker returns, since the picker needs the click's user activation.
  `isStreamingSave(sessions, SIO)` (internal) is the one test of which path
  `buildSlpBytes` takes, shared with the card. `saveProjectSlp` now sets
  `state.isSaving` and refuses a second save while one runs (two builds would
  hold two copies of the file in memory), and `quickSave` awaits it. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/save-progress-card.mjs`.
- Project: `newProject(force)` (`force` skips the unsaved-changes confirm and
  is used by the 3D-import reset), `markDirty`, `clearDirty`, `quickSave`,
  `saveAs`, `saveProjectSlp`, `saveProject`, `handleLoadProject` (routes a
  large `.slp` — over 200 MB, `LARGE_SLP_BYTES` — to the memory-bounded
  `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy` in `loading/session-loader.js` instead of the
  eager parse, freeing any previously loaded project first). A `.json` loaded
  WITHOUT video (Continue Without Video / `Esc` on `forceVideoSelection`) has no
  decoder to size it, so `state.totalFrames` comes from the last labeled frame
  (`adoptLabeledFrameCount`), the view opens on the first, and the status line
  says to attach videos via File ▸ Load Videos.
- `buildSlpBytes` (internal) assembles the multi-session SLP. **First, every
  UNCALIBRATED session gets a placeholder camera for each view, video and 2D
  camera key that has none** (`ensurePlaceholderCameras`, with
  `sessionViewsForSave` — `state.views` for the active session, its `_views`
  stash otherwise — and `sessionVideoFilesForSave` — its `videoFileIndices`, or
  the `vf.sessionIdx` tag Load Videos leaves). Every path below writes one video
  per `session.cameras` entry and drops any view or pose whose camera is
  missing, so a session with none (a folder loaded without calibration.toml)
  used to save as an EMPTY file. A session with a real calibration is never
  touched (`ensurePlaceholderCameras` returns early; `tests/test-placeholder-cameras.mjs`
  §5), so what it writes does not change. **When EVERY
  session in `state.sessions` has an open `lazyLoader`, it routes to the
  memory-bounded streaming path** — `buildSessionSlpBytesStreaming`
  (`import-export/slp-streaming-write.js`) for a single lazy session, or
  `saveAllSessionsStreaming(sessions)` (below) for multiple — instead of the
  eager `buildSlpLabelsAllViews` + `saveSlpToBytes` (which would OOM and
  silently drop every unvisited frame). A mixed project (any session without a
  `lazyLoader`, e.g. hand-labeled) falls through to the eager path, whose peak
  is the `Labels` graph rather than the writer (the vendored
  `writeLabeledFrames` appends through fixed-size buffers — `luc3d eager-save`
  patch — and `buildSlpLabelsAllViews` uses `makePooledSioInstances`).
  **Large-project size warning:** before entering the streaming path,
  `estimateSaveCagePressureBytes(sessionsToExport)` (exported) prices the
  RESIDENT state the merged save holds in V8's capped heap (per-object costs
  from `tests/e2e/_diag-repr-sizing.mjs`; typed-array backing stores live
  outside the cap and are not counted). Past
  `CAGE_WARN_FRACTION` (0.85) of `getCageLimitBytes()` (exported;
  `performance.memory.jsHeapSizeLimit`, else 4 GB) — set above a real project
  known to save at 77% —
  `window.confirm(...)` warns the user and offers to cancel in favor of
  per-camera export ("Export SLEAP File Per Session"/"By Cam"). Declining throws
  `SaveCancelledError`, which `quickSave`/`saveProjectSlp` report as "Save
  cancelled" (not "Save failed") — `quickSave` additionally calls
  `writable.abort()` on the already-open `FileSystemWritableFileStream`
  instead of `close()`, so a cancelled save doesn't overwrite the destination
  file with zero bytes. Bypass via `opts.skipSizeWarning` on `buildSlpBytes`.
  Covered by `tests/test-save-load-lazy-risk.js`.
- **`saveAllSessionsStreaming(sessions)`** / **`beginMultiSessionSave()`** +
  **`commitSessionForMultiSessionSave(handle, session)`** +
  **`finalizeMultiSessionSave(handle, opts)`** — the multi-session
  generalization of the streaming writer (see `slp-streaming-write.js`'s
  two-pass split). `saveAllSessionsStreaming` is the wrapper for when every
  session is already computed and resident. The three-piece API lets a caller interleave
  PASS 1's per-session commit (`commitSessionForMultiSessionSave` — builds the
  ref graph, then evicts that session's `lazyLoader`/`frameGroups`/
  `instanceGroups`) with that session's OWN compute step, one session at a
  time — the only way to bound peak memory when one session's compute alone
  approaches the tab's ceiling. **No UI currently drives that interactive
  per-session flow** (open → Track All → Triangulate All → commit → evict →
  next session) — `reopenSessionLazyLoader(session, sourceFileEntries,
  wasSharedStore)` (internal) supports it via `SioLazyLoader.sourceFiles`
  (retained `File` handles, so pass 2's restream doesn't need Track
  All/Triangulate All redone). For a shared-store project session (every
  camera's sourceFiles entry is the SAME `.slp`), it reopens via
  `SioLazyLoader.openProjectSlp` so the one interleaved store is read once and
  `_sharedStore`/`videoIdByCam` are restored — per-camera `open()` would
  re-read the project once per camera and pass 2 would append the shared store
  N times (duplicating every frame/track).
  `commitSessionForMultiSessionSave` records the flag as `sharedStore` on
  `handle.pending`, and `finalizeMultiSessionSave` passes it through.
  A per-camera session reopens through parallel `open()`s in `sourceFiles`
  order; order does not matter, since `SioLazyLoader._unifyTracks` re-indexes
  every store into the union of the cameras' track names in camera-NAME order —
  so pass 2 re-derives the track columns the original load had.
  **A store edited in memory is written AS EDITED.** Propagate IDs → Tracks
  (and every other `remapTracksFromIdentity` caller) and Custom Instance
  Delete (`deleteInstanceRows`) change the live store's columns, which are not
  in the source files — but pass 1 builds the header tracks, each camera's
  `trackBase` and every group's `(frame, instance offset)` refs from that live
  store; appending the files' columns under it would put propagated instances
  on the wrong track, resurrect deleted ones, and shift group refs onto deleted
  rows. So `commitSessionForMultiSessionSave` keeps the loader's `framesData` +
  `instancesData` per camera when `SioLazyLoader._storeEditedInMemory` is set
  (`keepEditedStoreColumns`, stored as `editedColumns` on `handle.pending`),
  and `finalizeMultiSessionSave` puts them back over the re-opened stores
  before `streamSessionIntoWriter` (`restoreEditedStoreColumns`). Those two are
  all either edit touches (a delete keeps every survivor's
  `point_id_start/end`), so `pointsData`/`predPointsData` are still evicted and
  re-read, and an UNEDITED session keeps nothing. `restoreEditedStoreColumns`
  throws if the re-opened file no longer fits the kept columns (another
  frame-row count, or a points table shorter than the largest kept
  `point_id_end`): the file changed on disk after loading, and writing edited
  instances against its rows would be silent corruption. The single-session
  save streams from the live loader and never needs this. Covered by
  `tests/e2e/multi-session-save-store-edits.mjs`.

  **GC between sessions.** Dereferencing a session's heavy state makes it
  only *eligible* for GC, so the multi-session flow streams to `opts.sink`
  (no extra whole-output buffer) and calls the private `encourageGC(totalMB)`
  (allocates toward the real heap ceiling, which forces the mark-compact pass a
  modest allocation would not) between sessions. A worker-per-session design
  is not possible: a module Worker cannot import
  `lib/sleap-io/index.browser.js` (its bare specifiers resolve only via the
  page's import map, which Workers do not inherit).

  Each session's
  `sessions_json` payload carries per-session `metadata.lucid.identities`
  (alongside `frameIdentityMap`/`tracks`), keeping identities scoped per
  session across save/load. The file-level `identities_json` remains a
  cross-session concatenation for SLEAP compatibility only. The file-level
  `allTracks` is a name-deduped union across sessions; after it is built,
  `buildSlpBytes` **re-points every instance's `track` to the canonical (first-
  seen) Track object for its name** so sleap-io's object-identity
  `tracks.indexOf(instance.track)` resolves it to the right global slot —
  otherwise a later session's instance on a shared-name track serializes as
  `-1` (trackless). (On load, the global slot is re-localized to the session's
  own track index by name — see `slp-import.js` / `remapGlobalTrackToSession`.)
- Status / overlay: `showLoading(msg)`, `hideLoading` (re-exported from
  `ui/loading-overlay.js`, which owns the overlay; kept here because ~10 modules
  import them from this one), `setStatus(text, type)`.

**Trackless (null track) preservation.** `_restoreProjectV2` restores grouped
and unlinked instances with `trackIdx = null` when the saved `trackIdx` is null
(not `0`), so a trackless instance stays trackless across a
project save/reload — matching the SLP import path in `slp-import.js`.
- 3D-import guard: `confirmDiscardImported3D()` (two-button warning modal,
  Promise<boolean>) and `ensureNo3dImportBlockingLoad()` — called at the top of
  the session-load entry points (`handleLoadProject`, `handleLoadSlpFile`,
  `handleLoadSessionFolder`). When `state.has3dImportWithoutSession` is set
  (3D points imported into a skeleton-only project), it warns and, on confirm,
  fully resets via `newProject(true)` so nothing — not even the skeleton —
  survives before the session loads. `newProject` clears the flag.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/pose-data.js`, `../pose/triangulation.js`,
  `../pose/placeholder-cameras.js` (`ensurePlaceholderCameras`),
  `../ui/browser-hints.js` (`fileSystemAccessHint` — `saveProjectSlp`, the
  download fallback when there is no save-file picker, says why and how to
  enable it in Brave), `../loading/video.js`, `../demo-data.js`, `./file-io.js`,
  `../ui/app-state.js`, `../loading/session-loader.js`,
  `../loading/sio-lazy-loader.js` (`SioLazyLoader`, for
  `reopenSessionLazyLoader`), `./slp-streaming-write.js`,
  `../ui/rendering.js`, `../ui/info-panel.js`,
  `../ui/frame-readout.js` (`clearReadout`, New Project),
  `../ui/loading-overlay.js` (`showLoading`/`hideLoading`, re-exported),
  `../ui/loading-progress-modal.js`,
  `../pose/initialization.js`, `../ui/sessions-panes.js`,
  `./slp-import.js`, `./visibility-metadata.js`
  (`writeVisibilityMetadata`/`readVisibilityMetadata` for the session-scoped
  settings in the legacy v2/v3 project JSON — same keys and omit-the-defaults
  rule as the `.slp` writers, at the session-dict level), `./plane-metadata.js`
  (`writePlaneMetadata`/`readPlaneMetadata`/`resetPlaneState`, same reason),
  `./smoothing-metadata.js` (Temporal Smoothing: `unsmoothedPoints3d` per group
  and the session-level key in the project JSON, same reason),
  `./slp-provenance.js` (`buildSaveProvenance` — every `.slp` project save keeps
  the loaded files' provenance).
- `../lib/sleap-io/index.browser.js` — dynamic `import()` in the private
  `ensureSleapIO()`, a fallback taken only when the `window.SleapIO` bridge in

  `../`; it read `./lib/…` (i.e. `import-export/lib/…`, a 404) until
  `tests/test-module-specifiers-resolve.mjs` was added to check every relative
  module reference in the app.

**Imported by.** `pose/triangulation.js`, `pose/tracker.js`,
`pose/initialization.js`, `import-export/slp-import.js`,
`loading/session-loader.js`, `ui/info-panel.js`,

### import-export/slp-import.js

**Purpose.** SLP/H5 project import + 3D-points-overlay import. Three
workflows: load fresh SLP (replaces state), additive merge SLP into
current session, overlay reprojected points3d from H5.

**campy recordings.** When the video prompt's folder is a campy recording
(session.json), `handleLoadSlpFile` puts it on the session
(`adoptCampyRecording`, `loading/session-loader.js`): a camera whose campy video
was picked loads only that video, views follow campy's camera order, the frame
rate is session.json's, and the final status gains each session's warnings
(`campyRecordingWarnings`). No calibration hash check here: the calibration
came from the `.slp`.

The `.slp` parse is dispatched by the private `parseSlpForImport(file,
onProgress)`: real `.slp` files go through `parseSlpViaSleapIO` (sleap-io.js
streaming reader), with `parseSlpH5` (raw h5wasm worker) kept for SLEAP
analysis `.h5` and as a fallback on any typed-read error. Both yield the same
`slpData` shape.

On load, identities are restored **per session**: each session prefers its
own `metadata.lucid.identities` (from `sessions_json`) and only falls back to
the file-level global `identities_json` for legacy/non-lucid SLPs, so IDs do
not leak across sessions and each session's `identity_idx` stays aligned with
its own list.

**Tracks are likewise per-session.** Each session takes a fresh **copy** of its
track list (`metadata.lucid.tracks.slice()`, else `slpData.tracks.slice()`), so
deleting a track in one session cannot hit the others through a shared array
(the per-session maxTrack padding also mutates it).

**Global→per-session track-index remap (critical).** The worker reads each
instance's track column as an index into the file-level GLOBAL track union
(`slpData.tracks`). For a lucid multi-session project (`hasPerSessionTracks`),
pass-1 translates it to THIS session's track index by NAME via
`remapGlobalTrackToSession`, and the `maxTrack` padding is SKIPPED. Using the
raw global index plus the padding corrupts tracks: deleting a track in one
session reorders the saved global union, and another session's instances then
pad phantom `track_N` names on reload (`global_0` → `track_3`).

**Trackless (null track) preservation.** A trackless instance is saved as
track `-1` (valid "no track" in SLEAP too). On re-import it stays trackless
(`trackIdx = null`) for **both user and predicted** instances: the raw-instance
path uses `resolveImportTrackIdx`, and the grouped-reconstruction path keeps
`instMeta.trackIdx` null — never defaulted to `0`, which would snap it onto the
first track.

**Key exports.**
- `handleLoadSlpFile(slpFile)` — replace-current-state load. Pre-allocates all
  N session groups in the LoadingProgressModal before the per-session loop so
  the header reads the true "Session n of N"; re-engages `showLoading` after the
  non-embedded folder picker so the overlay stays up during video decode. Calls
  `resetPlaneState()` up front, then `readVisibilityMetadata` /
  `readPlaneMetadata` per session. Skip-and-continue on per-session video-load
  failure (the failed session is dropped from `state.sessions`).
- `restoreGroupingAndUnlink(session, slpData, slpSessIdx, opts)` — restores a
  session's LUCID project state from the SLP's `sessions_json`: identities,
  `InstanceGroup` grouping, per-instance `nulledNodes`/occlusion, and 3D points
  (via `reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSession`/`...FromDicts`), then moves every
  ungrouped instance to the unlinked pool and de-dups pass-1 leftovers. The
  `session` must already hold pass-1 raw instances in its `FrameGroups`.
  Shared by `handleLoadSlpFile` and `handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp`
  (`loading/session-loader.js`) so both restore identically. It does NOT read
  the Visibility or Define Planes keys: only the caller knows whether the load
  replaces the project or adds a session, so each calls
  `readVisibilityMetadata` / `readPlaneMetadata` itself.
- Occlusion of **unlinked** user labels is restored in pass 1 by
  `nulledNodesFromOcclusion` (`import-export/import-track-resolve.js`).
- `handleAddSlp()` — additive merge into current session. A camera the
  session already has from the SAME video (`camerasAlreadyInSession`: same
  name and same `.slp` video path) is never added again — the old `<name>_2`
  copy turned a one-camera session into a two-camera one. If that camera has
  no view yet, it only asks for the video (Load SLP again after continuing
  without video); otherwise it changes nothing and says so. A same-named camera
  from another video (campy's `0.mp4`) is still added as `<name>_2`. Test:
  `tests/e2e/load-slp-same-video-twice.mjs`.
- `handleLoadPoints3dH5()` — overlay 3D points from H5. Requires only a loaded
  **skeleton** (not a full session): a camera-less skeleton-only project is
  accepted, the 3D viewport is force-created (bypassing the calibration gate)
  so the points render, and `state.has3dImportWithoutSession` is set so a later
  session load warns + resets (see `ensureNo3dImportBlockingLoad` in
  `save-load.js`). With no video to define a frame count, it adopts the file's
  duration (max `frame_indices` + 1) as `state.totalFrames` /
  `timeline.setTotalFrames` and calls `refreshReadoutTotals()` — NOT
  `updateTotalFrames()`, which reads decoder sample counts and would reset the
  count to 0. The H5 `track_names` / n-tracks dimension carries the
  identity/track assignment.
- `importSlpProjectWithProgress({ sessions, state, decoderFactory })` —
  testable multi-session load through the progress modal. Sessions load
  SEQUENTIALLY, each session's videos IN PARALLEL (`_loadSessionVideosParallel`);
  skip-and-continue per session. Also attached to `window` / `globalThis`.
- `reconstructInstanceGroupsFromDicts(session, fgDicts, camKeyToName, nodeNames, opts)`
  — async; rebuilds one session's `InstanceGroup`s + member `Instance`s from its
  saved `frame_group_dicts` (lucid grouping metadata in `sessions_json`),
  removing the matching pass-1 raw-SLP duplicates and restoring `points3d`.
  Exported so the grouped-reconstruction path is headlessly round-trip
  testable. Preserves trackless (`trackIdx` null) and identity-less
  (`identity_idx` -1) instances rather than defaulting them to 0.
  `opts.onProgress(msg)` receives batch progress; `opts.batch` (default 20000)
  sets the yield interval. Returns `{ restoredGroups, restoredWith3d }`. Used
  only for the raw-worker fallback path (files parsed by `parseSlpH5`).
- `reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSession(session, typedSession, rawSession, nodeNames, opts)`
  — async; the typed analog for `.slp` files read via `parseSlpViaSleapIO`.
  Rebuilds `InstanceGroup`s from the typed `RecordingSession`: 2D
  points/occlusion from each typed `Instance._xy`/`_visible`, per-instance
  metadata from `ig.metadata.lucid.instanceMeta`, 3D from `ig.instance3d.points`,
  the solver from `ig.metadata.lucid.triangulationMethod` (absent = `'dlt'`), and
  per-session identity from `ig.metadata.lucid.identityId` (else the raw dict's
  `identity_idx` for legacy files; else, with `opts.fileIdentities` — the typed
  `/identity` catalog carried on each `slpData.sessions[]` entry as
  `_typedIdentities`, passed by `restoreGroupingAndUnlink` only when the session's
  identities came from that catalog — the group's canonical `identity_idx`). Reads both LUCID's legacy and the canonical
  `sessions_json`. Same pass-1 dedup + trackless/identity-less handling as the
  dict version. Returns `{ restoredGroups, restoredWith3d }`.
  **Per-node model scores** (shown by the node tooltip): from the typed
  instance's `_score` here; in the dict version, from the raw-SLP instance a
  group member REPLACES (`adoptPointScoresFrom` — the group dict has none).
- `reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy(session, typedSession, loader, nodeNames, opts)`
  — async; the lazy-reopen analog (see "Lazy reopen's lightweight members"
  below). Imported by `loading/session-loader.js`. Identity per group from
  `metadata.lucid.identityId`; with `opts.fileIdentities` (the typed `/identity`
  catalog, passed only when `session.identities` was built from it) a group
  without one takes its canonical `identity_idx` (`typedIG.identity`).
  `opts.onCount(done, total)` (optional, awaited; frames) is the Open project
  card's counted hook, called at each yield and at the end; `opts.onProgress`
  still gets the old message. `stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups` takes the same
  `opts.onCount`.
- `stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups(session, opts)` — now DEFINED in
  `./frame-identity-metadata.js` and re-exported here for existing callers. For
  files whose identities live only on the groups (campy-live-export: file
  catalog + per-group `identity_idx`/`identityId`, no LUC3D
  `identities`/`frameIdentityMap`/`trackIdentityMap`) `restoreGroupingAndUnlink`
  and `handleLoadProjectSlpLazy` call it directly; without it the Identity
  timeline and colour by identity show nothing. Takes `opts.onCount(done, total)`
  (the Open project card's counted hook) as well as `opts.onProgress`. Test:
  `tests/e2e/lazy-reopen-group-identities.mjs`.
- `restoreGroupingAndUnlink` also calls `readFrameIdentityMetadata` right after
  the groups are rebuilt, which rebuilds `frameIdentityMap` from them when the
  file stored it in the compact form (see `import-export/frame-identity-metadata.js`).
- `parseSlpForImport(file, onProgress)` (private) — dispatches `.slp` →
  `parseSlpViaSleapIO`, else / on error → `parseSlpH5`.

**Private helpers (not exported).**
- `_loadSessionVideosParallel({ sessionIdx, session, state, modal, groupId, decoderFactory })`
  — fan-out per-video decoder loads via `Promise.allSettled`. Used by
  `importSlpProjectWithProgress` and the non-embedded path of
  `handleLoadSlpFile`.

**Project-load decoder pool reset.** At the top of `handleLoadSlpFile`,
closes every decoder in `state.decoderPool` and `state._decoderPoolCold`,
cancels every cold eviction timer, and re-initialises both arrays.

**Lazy reopen's lightweight members share ONE placeholder.**
`reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy` builds each group member WITHOUT its
2D (it is hydrated on scrub by `_rawInstIndex`, `finalizeLazyFrameGroup`); the
stand-in is the shared `lazyPlaceholderXY(numNodes)` (`pose/pose-data.js`),
adopted by reference — not a NaN-filled `Float64Array` per member, which costs
one ArrayBuffer each across millions of members. It is the same placeholder
`pose/lazy-residency.js` releases members back to, so a reopened project and a
freshly tracked one look alike off-screen.
Every restored group's 3D is copied into the slab pool (`pooledPoints3d`,
`pose/pose-data.js`) rather than kept as the reader's one-ArrayBuffer-per-group
`Float64Array`, which is released with its typed group.

**The load keeps only the 3D.** `handleLoadSlpFile` does not reproject at load
(~75 KB per frame per animal on a large project, for frames nobody looks at).
It only marks groups with 3D clean; `ui/rendering.js`'s reprojection fill
derives a frame's when it is shown, from the same stored 3D, and keeps at most
`DERIVED_FRAMES_KEEP` frames' worth. Two rules keep the result unchanged:
- **The file's 3D is reprojected, never re-solved.** The three restore sites
  (`reconstructInstanceGroupsFromDicts`, `...FromSession`,
  `...FromSessionLazy`) and `handleLoadPoints3dH5` mark each group's 3D with
  `markPoints3dFromFile` (`pose/pose-data.js`), and the fill reprojects marked
  3D whenever the group is clean — also with a reprojection-error threshold
  set, the one case in which it otherwise re-solves, and also for the `.h5`
  import's groups, which have no `triangulationMethod`. `handleLoadPoints3dH5`
  also drops a replaced group's derived state
  (`dropGroupDerivedReprojections`) so the old 3D's reprojections do not linger.
- **The Reprojections and Errors layers switch on** when the active session
  has 3D (`setReprojErrorVisible(true)`) — keyed on the 3D, not on the results
  map, which is empty until the first draw.
Covered by `tests/e2e/load-export-move-bounded-derived-state.mjs` (L).

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/pose-data.js` (incl. `lazyPlaceholderXY`, `markPoints3dFromFile`),
  `../pose/triangulation.js` (`getInstanceGroupsForFrame`),
  `../pose/placeholder-cameras.js` (`hasRealCalibration` — `handleLoadSlpFile`
  sets up the 3D viewer only for a real calibration, not for the placeholder
  cameras of a SLEAP file or of a project saved without one),
  `../pose/lazy-residency.js` (`dropGroupDerivedReprojections`), `./file-io.js`,
  `./slp-merge.js`, `../loading/video.js`, `../ui/app-state.js`,
  `../loading/session-loader.js`, `./save-load.js`,
  `../ui/rendering.js`, `../ui/info-panel.js`,
  `../ui/calibration-notice.js` (`noteSessionCalibrationDivergence`, behind the
  multi-session skeleton prompt — a `.slp` carries one calibration per session,
  so merging two separately-calibrated recordings lands here too),
  `../pose/initialization.js`, `../ui/ui-wiring.js`,
  `../ui/frame-readout.js` (`refreshReadoutTotals`, when a video-less import
  sets the frame count), `../ui/sessions-panes.js`, `./visibility-metadata.js`
  (`readVisibilityMetadata`), `./frame-identity-metadata.js`
  (`stampFrameIdentitiesFromGroups`, `readFrameIdentityMetadata`), `./plane-metadata.js` (`readPlaneMetadata`,
  `resetPlaneState`), `./smoothing-metadata.js` (`readSmoothingMetadata`, and
  `adoptSmoothingSource` in both typed group rebuilders), `./import-track-resolve.js`
  (`remapGlobalTrackToSession`, `nulledNodesFromOcclusion`),
  `./slp-provenance.js` (`fileSessionProvenance`, `fileSessionNames` — each
  session keeps its part of the file's provenance),
  `../loading/video-file-pick.js` (`preferNonCalibrationVideos`),
  `../ui/loading-progress-modal.js` (`getLoadingProgressModal`),
  `../loading/cold-read-guard.js`
  (`readBlobArrayBuffer`: Load 3D Points reads its `.h5` into memory, outside
  h5wasm's guarded WORKERFS). Also spawns
  `../loading/frame-worker.js` (twice) via `new Worker(new URL(...))`.

**Imported by.** `import-export/save-load.js`, `ui/ui-wiring.js`,
`loading/session-loader.js` (`restoreGroupingAndUnlink`,
`reconstructInstanceGroupsFromSessionLazy`).

**User-facing features.** File ▸ Load SLP (which merges via `handleAddSlp`
when a session is already open), File ▸ Load 3D Points (H5).

### import-export/import-track-resolve.js

**Purpose.** One pure (dependency-free) helper, `resolveImportTrackIdx(session,
rawTrackIdx, instType)`, that maps an imported instance's raw track index to
LUCID's internal representation. A trackless instance (`track = -1` or `null`)
stays trackless (`trackIdx = null`) for **both** user and predicted instances;
real track indices pass through. Defensively normalizes an unsigned-int32
readback of `-1` (`0xFFFFFFFF`) back to `-1`.

Lives here, not in `loading/session-loader.js` (which transitively imports
`app.js` and can't be bridged into the test runner), so it can be unit tested.
`session`/`instType` are retained in the signature but not consulted
(predictions must not be coerced trackless → 0).

Also exports `remapGlobalTrackToSession(rawTrackIdx, globalTrackNames,
sessionTrackNames)` — maps a per-instance track index from the file-level
(GLOBAL) track list (`tracks_json`) to a SPECIFIC session's track index, **by
name**, since tracks are per-session but a multi-session SLP stores one global
list. Without it, deleting a track in one session reorders the global union and
silently remaps another session's instances. Trackless stays trackless; a
global track absent from the session returns `-1`.
`slp-import.js` calls it in pass-1 for lucid multi-session projects; the
save-side counterpart (re-pointing instances to canonical Track objects so they
serialize to the right global slot) lives in `save-load.js` `buildSlpBytes`.

Also exports `nulledNodesFromOcclusion(points, occluded, type)` — rebuilds a
**user** instance's occlusion set (`nulledNodes`) from its saved per-point
occlusion (a point present in the file but flagged not-visible).
`_buildSioPoints` writes an occluded node as real-xy + `visible:false`, so
occlusion lives in the SLP as invisibility for BOTH grouped and unlinked
instances — but the explicit `nulledNodes` FLAG is only persisted in per-group
`instanceMeta` (grouped only), so an **unlinked** user label needs this to keep
its occlusion on reload. Called in the pass-1 raw-instance build of BOTH
`handleLoadSlpFile` and `handleLoadSessionFolderSingleSlp`. Predicted instances
are excluded (an invisible predicted point is low-confidence, not a user
occlusion). Covered by `tests/test-occlusion-derive.js`.

**Key exports.** `resolveImportTrackIdx`, `remapGlobalTrackToSession`,
`nulledNodesFromOcclusion`.

**Imported by.** `loading/session-loader.js` (`resolveImportTrackIdx`, which it
re-exports, and `nulledNodesFromOcclusion`),
`import-export/slp-import.js` (`remapGlobalTrackToSession`,
`nulledNodesFromOcclusion`; it takes `resolveImportTrackIdx` via
`session-loader.js`). Bridged into
`tests/test-runner.html`; covered by `tests/test-import-track-resolve.js`.

---

### import-export/slp-merge.js

**Purpose.** Pure helpers for additive multi-SLP loading — skeleton
compatibility check, track merging, frame merging, group rebuild.

**Key exports.**
- `validateSkeletonCompatibility(existing, incoming)` — returns
  `{error, reorderMap}`.
- `mergeTracksIntoSession(session, incomingTracks)`.
- `mergeSlpFramesIntoSession(session, slpData, videoIdxToCameraName,
  cameras, trackRemap, nodeReorderMap)` — trackless instances (track=-1/null),
  user OR predicted, keep `trackIdx = null`.
  A prediction keeps its per-node model scores (`pointScores`), re-ordered by
  `nodeReorderMap` exactly as its points are.
- `rebuildInstanceGroupsForFrames(session, frameIndices)` — groups by `trackIdx`;
  trackless instances of any type are skipped (not bucketed into track 0).
- `slpVideoByCamera(videos, videoIdxToCameraName)` — camera name → the video
  path the file gives (`sourceFilename`, else `filename`; '.' left out), kept
  on `Session.slpVideoByCamera`.
- `camerasAlreadyInSession(session, incoming)` — the incoming cameras the
  session already has: same name AND same recorded video path. `handleAddSlp`
  never adds these again.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `../pose/pose-data.js` — `Skeleton`, `Camera`, `Instance`,
  `InstanceGroup`, `FrameGroup`, `Session`.

**Imported by.** `import-export/slp-import.js`, `import-export/file-io.js`
(`validateSkeletonCompatibility`).

**User-facing features.** Backs File ▸ Load SLP's merge path (`handleAddSlp`) — merging an SLP into
an existing session without overwriting it.

## root

### app.js

**Purpose.** App entry. Two lines — imports `pose/initialization.js`,
which runs `init()` at module-load.

**Key exports.** None.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./pose/initialization.js`.

**Imported by.** Nothing (entry point loaded via `<script type="module">`
in `index.html`).

**User-facing features.** App boot.

### demo-data.js

**Purpose.** Generate synthetic Session, Skeleton, and Cameras for
"Load Demo Session" — a 4-camera mouse rig with a 6-node skeleton and
a circling-mouse animation noised over 3 of 4 views (top view left
empty so the user can practice triangulating).

**Key exports.**
- `createDemoCalibration()` — returns 4 calibrated `Camera` objects
  (back / mid / side / top).
- `createDemoSkeleton()` — `Skeleton.defaultMouse()`.
- `generateDemoKeypoints3D(numFrames)` — 3D-keypoint trajectories.
- `createDemoSession(numFrames=100)` — returns
  `{session: Session, keypoints3d}`.

**Imports from project modules.**
- `./pose/pose-data.js` — `Skeleton`, `Camera`, `Instance`,
  `FrameGroup`, `Session`, `UnlinkedInstance`.

**Imported by.** `pose/initialization.js` (`createDemoSession`, used by
`loadDemoSession`), `import-export/save-load.js` (`createDemoSkeleton`, the
fallback skeleton when loaded data carries none).

**User-facing features.** The synthetic demo dataset (`loadDemoSession`).

---

### server.py

**Purpose.** Development server (`SimpleHTTPRequestHandler`). Serves the static
tree, keeps a legacy `POST /convert-slp` endpoint alive when `h5py` is present,
and implements **offline mode**.

**CLI.** `python3 server.py [port] [--offline]`; `LUCID_OFFLINE=1` also enables
offline. Prints which mode it started in.

**Offline mode.** Reads `offline-deps.json` via `scripts/offline_deps.py` and
rewrites the mapped CDN URLs in served `.html` / `.js` / `.mjs` bodies to
depth-relative `lib/` paths (`./lib/…` at root, `../lib/…` from `tests/`). Files
on disk are never modified, so the working tree keeps its CDN URLs. Refuses to
start when a declared package is missing rather than serving a page that half
works. `.js` coverage is required, not optional: two of the six CDN references
are ESM imports inside `ui/sessions-panes.js` and `ui/overlay-export-modal.js`.

**Imports from project modules.** `scripts/offline_deps.py` —
`load_manifest`, `package_status`, `replacement_map`, `rewrite_text`,
`is_rewritable`.

**User-facing features.** Local development; offline development.

---

### scripts/offline_deps.py

**Purpose.** Fetch, verify and vendor the four CDN dependencies declared in
`offline-deps.json` into gitignored `lib/` directories, and build a
pre-rewritten offline bundle. Standard library only — no Node, curl, tar or
Git Bash, so it behaves identically on Linux, macOS and Windows.

**CLI.** `install` (idempotent; `--force` re-fetches) · `check`
(`--strict` also fails on unmapped CDN URLs) · `bundle` · `clean`.
Honors `LUCID_NPM_REGISTRY`.

**Key functions.** `load_manifest()`, `replacement_map()`, `rewrite_text()`
(shared with `server.py`), `package_status()`, `run_asserts()` (behavioral
checks pinning the API each package must still expose), `check_pins()`
(re-derives the dockview version pin from source rather than trusting prose),
`find_unmapped_cdn_urls()`, `is_rewritable()` (shared with `server.py`; skips Node-only
`.mjs` under `tests/` and `scripts/`).

**Imported by.** `server.py`; `tests/e2e/offline-server.mjs` shells out to it.

**User-facing features.** Offline install and the distributable bundle.
